Arithmetic compactifications of integral models of Shimura varieties of abelian type
Peihang Wu

Abstract
. In this paper, we construct good toroidal and minimal compactifications in the sense of Lan-Stroh for integral models of abelian-type Shimura varieties. We start with finding suitable types of cusp labels and cone decompositions which are compatible with those of the associated Hodge-type Shimura varieties. We then study the action of ℚ \mathbb{Q} -points of the adjoint group on boundary charts and toroidal compactifications of Hodge-type integral models. In particular, we extend the twisting construction of Kisin and Pappas to boundary charts. Finally, up to taking refinements of cone decompositions, we construct an abelian-type toroidal compactification as an open and closed algebraic subspace of a quotient from a disjoint union of Hodge-type toroidal compactifications and construct minimal compactifications with a similar method. Furthermore, we show results on nearby cycles of these compactifications and verify Pink’s formula when the level at p p is an intersection of n n quasi-parahoric subgroups.

Introduction

In this paper, we will study the Shimura varieties associated with abelian-type Shimura data and construct compactifications of certain integral models associated with them.

To recall the definitions, a pair
(G,X)
of a connected reductive
\mathbb{Q}
-group
G
together with a
G(\mathbb{R})
-conjugacy class of homomorphisms
\mathbb{S}:=R_{\mathbb{C}/\mathbb{R}}\mathbb{G}_{m}\to G_{\mathbb{R}}
is called a
Shimura datum
if it satisfies
[
20
, 2.1.1.1-2.1.1.3]
(see also
[
55
, Def. 5.5]
).
Choosing a neat open compact subgroup
K\subset G(\mathbb{A}_{f})
, the complex double coset space
\mathrm{Sh}_{K}(G,X)(\mathbb{C}):=G(\mathbb{Q})\backslash X\times G(\mathbb{A}_{f})/K
uniquely algebraizes to a complex algebraic variety
\mathrm{Sh}_{K}(G,X)_{\mathbb{C}}
by
[
6
]
and
[
8
]
.
Furthermore, by the works of Shimura, Deligne, Milne, Borovoi and others, the inverse system of algebraic varieties
\{\mathrm{Sh}_{K}(G,X)_{\mathbb{C}}\}_{K}
with varying neat open compact subgroups
K
canonically descends to an inverse system of algebraic varieties
\{\mathrm{Sh}_{K}(G,X)\}_{K}
over a number field (called the
reflex field
)
E(G,X)
contained in
\mathbb{C}
, such that the Galois action on special points is given by a certain reciprocity law of the class field theory.

Abelian-type Shimura varieties and their integral models are important in number theory, and there are interesting problems and applications related to them. For an abelian-type Shimura datum
(G_{2},X_{2})
, the weight cocharacter and the center of the reductive group are more complicated than those of a Hodge-type Shimura datum; more precisely, the Axioms (
\mathrm{SV4}
), (
\mathrm{SV}5
) and (
\mathrm{SV}6
) in
[
55
, Sec. 5]
are not satisfied in general. To study an abelian-type Shimura variety, one can study a Hodge-type Shimura variety associated with it first; the two varieties are related by a quotient over geometrically connected components and by an
induction construction
of Deligne
[
20
]
.

Let
p
be a prime number. Let
(G_{2},X_{2})
be an abelian-type Shimura datum. By definition (see, e.g.,
[
36
, Intro.]
), it is associated with a Hodge-type Shimura datum
(G_{0},X_{0})
. Assume that
K_{2}=K_{2,p}K^{p}_{2}
, where
K_{2,p}
is an open compact subgroup of
G_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
and
K_{2}^{p}
is a neat open compact subgroup of
G_{2}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
. When
G_{2}
is quasi-split and unramified at
p
and
K_{2,p}
is hyperspecial, it was shown by Kisin and Kim-Madapusi that
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{2}}(G_{2},X_{2})
extends to a
smooth integral model

\mathcal{S}_{K_{2}}
, and
\mathcal{S}_{K_{2,p}}:=\varprojlim\mathcal{S}_{K_{2,p}K^{p}_{2}}
is
canonical
in the sense that it satisfies
the extension property
(see
[
36
, 2.3.7]
and
[
31
]
).
When
p>2
and
K_{2,p}
is parahoric, Kisin, Pappas, and Zhou showed that
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{2,p}}(G_{2},X_{2})
extends to an integral model
\mathcal{S}_{K_{2,p}}(G_{2},X_{2})
, which satisfies a weaker extension property and has a
local model diagram
(see
[
33
]
and
[
32
]
).
Moreover, these integral models
\mathcal{S}_{K_{2,p}}
are canonical in the sense that they satisfy the
conjecture of Pappas and Rapoport
(see
[
63
, Conj. 4.2.2]
,
[
13
]
and
[
14
, Thm. 4.10]
).

Background on compactifications

Shimura varieties are not proper in general, and they admit smooth and projective toroidal compactifications and projective minimal compactifications over reflex fields (see
[
6
]
,
[
5
]
and
[
64
]
). In many occasions, studying the integral models of these compactifications is very beneficial.

It is conjectured that smooth integral models of Shimura varieties of abelian type have toroidal and minimal compactifications with the same properties mentioned above extending those over the reflex fields (see
[
54
, Conj. 2.18]
). Such compactifications of smooth integral models were constructed by Chai and Faltings
[
21
]
in the Siegel-type case, by Lan
[
44
]
in the PEL-type case and by Madapusi
[
50
]
in the Hodge-type case.

In fact, one can expect a similar story for more general integral models. Let us fix a prime number
p
and continue with the conventions above. As before,
(G_{0},X_{0})
by definition admits an embedding (called Hodge embedding in this paper)
\iota:(G_{0},X_{0})\hookrightarrow(G^{\ddagger},X^{\ddagger})
, i.e., an injective group homomorphism
G_{0}\hookrightarrow G^{\ddagger}:=\mathrm{GSp}(V,\psi)
, which induces an embedding from
X_{0}
to the union of Siegel upper and lower half-spaces
X^{\ddagger}:=\mathbb{S}^{\pm}
. Suppose that we choose a self-dual lattice
V_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}
of
V_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
and an open compact subgroup
K_{0,p}\subset G_{0}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
such that
K_{0,p}\subset K_{p}^{\ddagger}:=\mathrm{Stab}_{G^{\ddagger}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})}(V_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}).

Let
K_{0}^{p}
be a neat open compact subgroup of
G_{0}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
contained in a neat open compact subgroup
K^{{\ddagger},p}
of
G^{\ddagger}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
. Set
K^{\ddagger}:=K_{p}^{\ddagger}K^{{\ddagger},p}
and
K_{0}:=K_{0,p}K^{p}_{0}
. Let
E_{0}
be the reflex field of
(G_{0},X_{0})
. Let
\mathcal{O}:=\mathcal{O}_{E_{0},(v)}
for a place
v|p
. Let
\mathcal{S}_{K_{0}}
be the normalization of
\mathcal{S}_{K^{\ddagger},\mathcal{O}}
in
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{0}}:=\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{0}}(G_{0},X_{0})
, where
\mathcal{S}_{K^{\ddagger}}
is the integral model of
\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{\ddagger}}(G^{\ddagger},X^{\ddagger})
over
{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}
constructed as a moduli space of polarized abelian schemes with level structures. Then
\mathcal{S}_{K_{0}}
is a quasi-projective normal scheme that is flat over
\mathcal{O}
, and its generic fiber is
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{0}}
.
For a choice of Hodge embedding

(G_{0},X_{0},K_{0,p})\hookrightarrow(G^{\ddagger},X^{\ddagger},K_{p}^{\ddagger})

such that
K_{p}^{\ddagger}\cap G_{0}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\neq K_{0,p}
, or a choice of open compact subgroup
K_{0,p}
beyond quasi-parahoric subgroups, the scheme
\mathcal{S}_{K_{0,p}}:=\varprojlim_{K_{0}^{p}}\mathcal{S}_{K_{0}}
is
not
canonical in the sense of satisfying the extension property or the conjecture of Pappas and Rapoport.

Despite this, since
[
45
]
and
[
50
]
, it has been understood that there are good toroidal and minimal compactifications associated with reasonably defined integral models of Shimura varieties of Hodge type, even when the integral models are not constructed with (quasi-)parahoric level structures or with any definition of canonicity mentioned above.

In fact, Madapusi proved that
all
Hodge-type integral models with hyperspecial or (quasi-)parahoric levels studied in
[
36
]
,
[
33
]
,
[
32
]
,
[
63
]
and
[
13
]
have good toroidal compactifications, and his results can also be applied in a broader context beyond quasi-parahoric levels with minimal restrictions on
K_{0,p}\subset K_{p}^{\ddagger}
(see
[
50
, Sec. 3.1]
). Let
\Sigma^{\ddagger}
be a smooth admissible rational polyhedral cone decomposition for
(G^{\ddagger},X^{\ddagger},K^{\ddagger})
. By
[
21
]
and
[
44
]
, there is a smooth toroidal compactification
\mathcal{S}_{K^{\ddagger}}^{\Sigma^{\ddagger}}
for
\mathcal{S}_{K^{\ddagger}}
. Let
\Sigma_{0}
be the cone decomposition for
(G_{0},X_{0})
induced by
\Sigma^{\ddagger}
. Define
\mathcal{S}_{K_{0}}^{\Sigma_{0}}
to be the normalization of
\mathcal{S}_{K^{\ddagger}}^{\Sigma^{\ddagger}}
in
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{0}}
.
Madapusi showed that
\mathcal{S}_{K_{0}}^{\Sigma_{0}}
has the right properties as a toroidal compactification, that is,
\mathcal{S}_{K_{0}}^{\Sigma_{0}}
has a good stratification and the complete local rings of
\mathcal{S}^{\Sigma_{0}}_{K_{0}}
at its boundary points are complete localizations of toric torsors, which can be explicitly described by integral models of mixed Shimura varieties (see
[
50
, Thm. 3.4.3 and Thm. 4.1.5]
). The generic fiber of
\mathcal{S}_{K_{0}}^{\Sigma_{0}}
is
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{0}}^{\Sigma_{0}}
, the toroidal compactification of
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{0}}
associated with the cone decomposition
\Sigma_{0}
by Pink’s characteristic
0
theory
[
64
]
. Similar results were also proved by Lan in
[
45
]
when
(G_{0},X_{0})
is of PEL type.

Hence, one expects that, for all abelian-type Shimura data, a good compactification theory should exist for integral models defined in an appropriate way with arbitrary levels at
p
.

Main results

Let
\Sigma_{2}
be an admissible rational polyhedral cone decomposition for
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{2}}:=\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{2}}(G_{2},X_{2})
. Denote by
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{2}}^{\Sigma_{2}}
the toroidal compactification of
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{2}}
associated with
\Sigma_{2}
by Pink’s theory. Denote
E_{2}:=E(G_{2},X_{2})
. Fix any place
v_{2}|p
of
E_{2}
.
The main goal of this paper is to construct good toroidal compactifications
\mathcal{S}_{K_{2}}^{\Sigma_{2}^{\prime}}
over
\mathcal{O}_{E_{2},v_{2}}
associated with suitable refinements
\Sigma_{2}^{\prime}
of
\Sigma_{2}
based on Madapusi’s results in the Hodge-type case.

The main results can be stated in the following compressed form. The readers will find precise statements in Theorem
4.39
.

Theorem A

(Theorem
4.39
)
.

Assume that
K_{2,p}
is an open compact subgroup of
G_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
and
K_{2}^{p}
is neat open compact in
G_{2}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
. Let
K_{2}=K_{2,p}K_{2}^{p}
. Let
v_{2}|p
be a place of
E_{2}
over
p
.

For any admissible cone decomposition
\Sigma_{2}
for
(G_{2},X_{2})
and
K_{2}
, there is a refinement
\Sigma_{2}^{\prime}
of
\Sigma_{2}
, which can be made smooth and/or projective, such that there is a proper normal flat model
\mathcal{S}_{K_{2}}^{\Sigma_{2}^{\prime}}
over
\mathcal{O}_{E_{2},v_{2}}
extending the toroidal compactification
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{2}}^{\Sigma^{\prime}_{2}}
constructed by Pink in
[
64
]
.
The compactification
\mathcal{S}^{\Sigma_{2}^{\prime}}_{K_{2}}
has a good stratification, which extends the stratification obtained from Pink’s theory from the generic fiber to the integral model.

There is an open dense subscheme
\mathcal{S}_{K_{2}}
in
\mathcal{S}_{K_{2}}^{\Sigma_{2}^{\prime}}
extending
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{2}}
, and the reduced complement
D
of
\mathcal{S}_{K_{2}}
in
\mathcal{S}^{\Sigma_{2}^{\prime}}_{K_{2}}
is a relative effective Cartier divisor. The complete local ring of
\mathcal{S}^{\Sigma_{2}^{\prime}}_{K_{2}}
at any point
x
of
D
is isomorphic to a complete local ring of a toric scheme. These toric schemes are constructed from finite quotients of the toric schemes associated with certain integral models of boundary mixed Shimura varieties of abelian type.

When
G_{2}
is quasi-split and unramified at
p
, and
K_{2,p}
is hyperspecial, the towers of integral models of boundary mixed Shimura varieties mentioned in the last paragraph can be constructed to satisfy the extension property. In this case,
\mathcal{S}^{\Sigma_{2}^{\prime}}_{K_{2}}
and its strata are defined over
\mathcal{O}_{E_{2},(v_{2})}
.

We can also construct a minimal (i.e., Satake-Baily-Borel) compactification
\mathcal{S}_{K_{2}}^{\mathrm{min}}
for
\mathcal{S}_{K_{2}}
. More precisely,

Theorem B

(Theorem
5.15
)
.

There is a normal projective model
\mathcal{S}_{K_{2}}^{\mathrm{min}}
over
\mathcal{O}_{E_{2},v_{2}}
(or over
\mathcal{O}_{E_{2},(v_{2})}
if
G_{2}
is quasi-split and unramified at
p
, and
K_{2,p}
is hyperspecial) extending the minimal compactification
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{2}}^{\mathrm{min}}
of
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{2}}
over
E_{2}
. There is a proper surjective morphism
\oint_{K_{2}}^{\Sigma_{2}^{\prime}}:\mathcal{S}^{\Sigma_{2}^{\prime}}_{K_{2}}\to\mathcal{S}_{K_{2}}^{\mathrm{min}}
with geometrically connected fibers, under which the stratifications on the source and the target compatibly extend the stratifications on the generic fibers.

As the aforementioned works
[
45
]
and
[
50
]
by Lan and Madapusi, our results also allow the level at
p
to be arbitrarily high (and low). More precisely, to apply our results, one can pick an open compact subgroup
K_{2,p}
and choose
n
Bruhat-Tits stabilizer subgroups
K_{2,p}^{i}
containing
K_{2,p}
from possibly distinct apartments (which means that
K_{2,p}
can be any proper open compact subgroup of
\cap_{i=1}^{n}K_{2,p}^{i}
), then a good compactification theory can be established by the constructions in this paper. Note that the compactifications and their stratifications obtained will depend on the choice of the set of Bruhat-Tits stabilizers, since in our article the models of the compactifications and stratifications at level
K_{2,p}K_{2}^{p}
are constructed by taking relative normalizations from the models at level
(\cap_{i=1}^{n}K_{2,p}^{i})\cdot K_{2}^{p}
, and the choice of the latter level structure can be flexible.

Remark
.

Since the integral models of compactifications in this paper have good stratifications, one can take the open dense strata (i.e., the interiors) of compactifications to get integral models of Shimura varieties.

In particular, this paper constructed integral models of abelian-type Shimura varieties with quasi-parahoric level structures at
p>0
. In a forthcoming work joint with Shengkai Mao (see Remark
5.24
), we show these models are canonical integral models in the sense of Pappas and Rapoport. In fact, we show that
p
-adic shtukas admit “canonical extensions” to integral models of toroidal compactifications of abelian-type Shimura varieties.

Another advantage of our results is that we have proved that the compactifications of abelian-type integral models also satisfy (a slightly generalized version of) the qualitative descriptions of good compactifications in the sense of Lan-Stroh (see, e.g.,
[
42
, Prop. 2.2]
). The only difference is that the “
\Gamma_{\sigma}
” in the notation of
loc. cit.
is in general not trivial in our case. In other words, in the Hodge-type or PEL-type case, the formal completions at the boundary can be described by (integral models of) mixed Shimura varieties defined by some irreducible mixed Shimura data, while for the abelian-type case, those completions are (also explicitly) described by
finite quotients
of (integral models of) those mixed Shimura varieties (see Remark
4.40
). Hence, one can immediately extend many results from the Hodge-type case to the abelian-type case, once the arguments to prove them only involve a good description of the boundary.

As an application, we generalize the nearby cycle results in
[
41
]
to the abelian-type case at all levels.

Proposition

(Proposition
5.21
and Corollary
5.23
)
.

Fix a prime
l\neq p
.
Let
\mathcal{V}
be a lisse
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{l}
-sheaf associated with an algebraic representation
\xi
of
G^{c}_{2}
on a finite-dimensional
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{l}
-vector space
V_{\xi}
or a finite
\mathbb{F}_{l}
-sheaf equipped with an action of an open compact subgroup
G_{2}^{c}(\mathbb{Z}_{l})
.

Assume that the projection of
K_{2}
into
G^{c}_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{l})
factors through
G^{c}_{2}(\mathbb{Z}_{l})
. Then there are natural isomorphisms induced by adjunctions that are equivariant under the actions of the absolute Galois group
\mathrm{Gal}(\overline{\eta}/\eta)
:

(1)

R\Psi_{\mathcal{S}_{K_{2}}^{\Sigma_{2}}}RJ_{\eta,*}^{\Sigma_{2}}\mathcal{V}\xrightarrow{\sim}RJ_{\overline{s},*}^{\Sigma_{2}}R\Psi_{\mathcal{S}_{K_{2}}}\mathcal{V}
and
J_{\overline{s},!}^{\Sigma_{2}}R\Psi_{\mathcal{S}_{K_{2}}}\mathcal{V}\xrightarrow{\sim}R\Psi_{\mathcal{S}_{K_{2}}^{\Sigma_{2}}}J_{\eta,!}^{\Sigma_{2}}\mathcal{V}
; similar results also hold for minimal compactifications.

(2)

R\Psi_{\mathcal{S}_{K_{2}}^{\mathrm{min}}}J_{,\eta,!*}^{\mathrm{min}}\mathcal{V}[d]\xrightarrow{\sim}J_{\overline{s},!*}^{\mathrm{min}}R\Psi_{\mathcal{S}_{K_{2}}}\mathcal{V}[d]
.

As another application, we prove Pink’s formula when the level at
p
is an intersection of
n
quasi-parahoric subgroups.

Proposition

(See Proposition
5.19
for details)
.

Fix a positive integer
n
.
Fix
n
Bruhat-Tits stabilizer subgroups
K^{i}_{2,p}
of
G_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
. Let
K_{2,p}
be an open compact subgroup of
G_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
such that
\cap_{i=1}^{n}K^{i,\circ}_{2,p}\subset K_{2,p}\subset\cap_{i=1}^{n}K^{i}_{2,p}
. Then Pink’s formula holds for the minimal compactification
\mathcal{S}^{\mathrm{min}}_{K_{2}}
constructed as the relative normalization from the integral model of the minimal compactification at level
\cap_{i=1}^{n}K^{i}_{2,p}
, where the latter one is constructed as in Case (
\mathrm{STB}_{n}
) for
\{K_{2,p}^{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}
.

Method.

From the definition of abelian-type Shimura data, we make the following construction.

Lemma

(See §
1.4.4
)
.

There is a connected reductive group
G
and two Shimura data
(G,X_{a})
and
(G,X_{b})
, such that
G^{\mathrm{der}}=G_{2}^{\mathrm{der}}
, such that
X_{a}
and
X_{b}
differ only by a homomorphism from
\mathbb{S}
to
Z_{G,\mathbb{R}}
(which we denote by
(G,X_{a})\sim(G,X_{b})
below to refer to this similarity), and such that there is a diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(G_{0},X_{0})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(G_{2},X_{2})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(G,X_{a})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(G,X_{b})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi^{b}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi^{a}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}

where
\pi^{a}
is an embedding such that
\pi^{a}(G_{2}^{\mathrm{der}})=G^{\mathrm{der}}
and
\pi^{b}
is a map whose kernel is the kernel of the central isogeny
G_{0}^{\mathrm{der}}\to G^{\mathrm{der}}=G_{2}^{\mathrm{der}}
.

This is a variant of the construction in
[
20
]
. For the topic of this paper, we found some advantages of doing the construction above: First of all, since the difference between the descent data of the objects associated with
(G,X_{a})
and
(G,X_{b})
is essentially induced by a central homomorphism
c:\mathbb{S}\to Z_{G,\mathbb{R}}
(which is explicitly determined by the reciprocity law mentioned above) and since there is a direct map
(G_{0},X_{0})\to(G,X_{b})
, we do not need to break up the integral model of
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{0}}(G_{0},X_{0})
into geometrically connected components to construct strata and compactifications for
(G_{2},X_{2})
.

Secondly, to construct integral models with the strategy above, we do not rely on certain parahoric
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-group schemes associated with
G
,
G_{0}
and
G_{2}
to construct the groups used in the induction construction. This gives us the flexibility we need in order to find suitable integral models when the level structure is arbitrary.

Another advantage is that the morphism from
(G_{2},X_{2})
to
(G,X_{a})
is an embedding. By choosing a suitable level
K\subset G(\mathbb{A}_{f})
, one can show that the morphisms induced by
(G_{2},X_{2})\hookrightarrow(G,X_{a})
between compactifications and strata are also embeddings. In particular, results for
(G_{2},X_{2})
can be immediately deduced from those for
(G,X_{a})
.

Let us now sketch some crucial steps in this article.

Cusp labels and cone decompositions.

Let
(G,X)
be any Shimura datum. For each cusp label representative
\Phi
(see
[
64
]
and
[
50
]
), Pink associated with
\Phi
a
\mathbb{Q}
-subgroup
P_{\Phi}
(which is denoted by
P_{1}
in
[
64
]
), which is a normal subgroup of the admissible
\mathbb{Q}
-parabolic
Q_{\Phi}
associated with
\Phi
(see
[
64
, Def. 4.5 and Prop. 4.6]
).

Note that there is in general no direct map between
P_{\Phi}
’s for
(G_{0},X_{0})
and
(G_{2},X_{2})
since there is no morphism from
(G_{0},X_{0})
to
(G_{2},X_{2})
.
To address the issue, let
ZP_{\Phi}
be the identity component of the group generated by the center
Z_{G}
of
G
and the group
P_{\Phi}
.
We use the following weaker equivalence relation to create coarser classes of cusp labels.

Definition

(Definition
1.28
)
.

Let
\Phi_{1}
and
\Phi_{2}
be two cusp label representatives. Then we say

\Phi_{1}\preceq\Phi_{2}
if there are
q^{\prime}\in ZP_{\Phi_{2}}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
and
\gamma\in G(\mathbb{Q})
such that

\gamma P_{\Phi_{1}}\gamma^{-1}\subset P_{\Phi_{2}}
,
P_{\Phi_{1}}(\mathbb{Q})\gamma X^{+}_{\Phi_{1}}=P_{\Phi_{2}}(\mathbb{Q})X^{+}_{\Phi_{2}}
and
\gamma g_{\Phi_{1}}\equiv q^{\prime}g_{\Phi_{2}}
modulo
K
.

A stratum defined by the equivalence relation above is actually a disjoint union of some strata in Pink’s definition; by considering this kind of disjoint union, we can uniformize the construction of strata and mixed Shimura varieties for
(G,X_{a})
and
(G,X_{b})
.

Remark
.

In fact, this is an example of an alternative definition of boundary components, where the mixed Shimura data produced are not irreducible (see
[
64
, Rmk. 4.11]
and §
1.1.1
).

More specifically, we can find cone decompositions as follows:

Proposition

(Proposition
1.47
)
.

We can choose a common cone decomposition
\Sigma
for both
(G,X_{a})
and
(G,X_{b})
, such that
\Sigma
induces a cone decomposition
\Sigma_{2}^{\prime}
, which is a refinement of
\Sigma_{2}
that can be made smooth, projective, or both smooth and projective, and such that
\Sigma
also induces cone decompositions
\Sigma_{0}^{\alpha}
for
(G_{0},X_{0})
under the pullback of
\pi^{b}(\alpha)
for each
\alpha
in an index set
I_{G/G_{0}}:=\mathrm{Stab}_{G(\mathbb{Q})}(X_{0})\pi^{b}(G_{0}(\mathbb{A}_{f}))\backslash G(\mathbb{A}_{f})/K
.

Construction.

We now have obtained the following diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(G_{0},X_{0});\Sigma_{0}^{\alpha}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(G_{2},X_{2});\Sigma_{2}^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(G,X_{a});\Sigma}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(G,X_{b});\Sigma.}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}

Note that one cannot get a good theory if one applies Deligne’s induction construction directly to toroidal compactifications; one reason is that one cannot choose smooth cone decompositions for general level
K_{2}
if cone decompositions over all geometrically connected components of the Shimura variety
\mathrm{Sh}(G_{2},X_{2})
are chosen the same. Hence, we first construct the toroidal compactification
\mathcal{S}^{\Sigma}_{K}(G,X_{b})
of
(G,X_{b})
as a disjoint union of certain quotients of Hodge-type toroidal compactifications associated with possibly different cone decompositions
\Sigma_{0}^{\alpha}
. We then construct
\mathcal{S}_{K_{2}}^{\Sigma_{2}^{\prime}}
as an open and closed algebraic subspace of
\mathcal{S}_{K}^{\Sigma}(G,X_{a})
. The latter one is isomorphic to
\mathcal{S}_{K}^{\Sigma}(G,X_{b})
after a possibly ramified normalized base change to a ring
\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}
, but the ramification of this normalized base change is controlled by
c
and the intersection of the center
Z_{G}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
and the level group
K_{p}\subset G(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
at
p
. For example, this (normalized) base change is unramified if
Z_{G}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\cap K_{p}
contains the parahoric subgroup of
Z_{G}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
.

We can show the following statement:

Proposition

(Corollary
4.55
)
.

After taking the normalized base change to
\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}
,
\mathcal{S}_{K_{2},\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}^{\Sigma_{2}^{\prime}}
is an open and closed subspace of
\mathcal{S}_{K,\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}^{\Sigma}(G,X_{b})
, and
\mathcal{S}_{K,\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}^{\Sigma}(G,X_{b})
, equipped with the log structure defined by the complement of
\mathcal{S}_{K,\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}(G,X_{b})
in
\mathcal{S}_{K,\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}^{\Sigma}(G,X_{b})
, admits a finite Kummer étale cover of disjoint union of Hodge-type toroidal compactifications
\mathcal{S}^{\mathrm{TOR}}_{K_{0},\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}:=\coprod_{\alpha\in I_{G/G_{0}}}\mathcal{S}^{\Sigma_{0}^{\alpha}}_{K_{0}^{\alpha},\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}
(with
K_{0,p}^{\alpha}
chosen depending on
K_{p}
). The induced map between interiors
\coprod_{\alpha\in I_{G/G_{0}}}\mathcal{S}_{K_{0}^{\alpha},\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}\to\mathcal{S}_{K,\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}(G,X_{b})
is finite étale.

Note that we show the statement above for any choice of
(G_{0},X_{0})
attached to
(G_{2},X_{2})
that satisfies a condition on reflex fields (see
[
33
, Lem. 4.6.22 (3)]
).

There are two main difficulties in the quotient construction.

The first difficulty is to find certain
\mathscr{A}
- and
\mathscr{A}^{\circ}
-type groups for mixed Shimura varieties, with which one can construct integral models of toroidal compactifications and integral models of boundary mixed Shimura varieties as (disjoint unions of) quotients.
Here we have to use a method that is different from
[
36
]
and
[
33
]
since we should construct the integral models and describe them at every finite level
K_{p}K^{p}
instead of describing at level
K_{p}
with
K_{p}
a parahoric subgroup.
An important input to achieve this is constructing a collection of integral models
\{\mathcal{S}_{{}^{\gamma}{K_{\Phi_{0}^{\alpha}}}}\}
such that the whole
G^{\mathrm{ad}}(\mathbb{Q})
acts on it.
In Section
4
, our proof of this crucially relies on the canonicity results in
[
36
]
,
[
31
]
,
[
63
]
,
[
14
]
and
[
13
]
and a detailed computation of the level groups associated with the boundary components in
[
51
]
.
Even though there might be other ways to obtain this, the advantage of this approach is that we can establish a stronger statement along the way on how the construction of the toroidal compactification
\mathcal{S}_{K_{0}^{\alpha}}^{\Sigma_{0}^{\alpha}}
is independent of the choice of Hodge embedding with prescribed formation.

The second difficulty is to show that the integral models of boundary mixed Shimura varieties are correct torus torsors.
To do this, we use twisting of
1
-motives to give an explicit moduli description of certain actions following the idea of Kisin in
[
36
]
.
The main result on twisting can be described as follows:

Proposition

(Proposition
3.24
)
.

Let
G_{0,{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}
(resp.
Z_{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}
) be the closure of
G_{0}
(resp.
Z_{G_{0}}
) in
G^{\ddagger}_{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}
. Let
G^{\mathrm{ad}}_{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}:=G_{0,{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}/Z_{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}
. For any
\gamma\in G^{\mathrm{ad}}_{0,{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}({\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}})_{1}
and any cusp label representative
\Phi=(Q,X_{0}^{+},g^{\ddagger})
as in
[
50
]
, there is a twisted cusp label representative
\Psi
defined as
\Psi:=(\gamma Q\gamma^{-1},\gamma(X_{0}^{+}),\gamma g^{\ddagger}\gamma^{-1})
, such that the action

\gamma^{-1}:\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi}}(\mathbb{C})\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Psi}}(\mathbb{C}),

sending
\wp=[(x,\mathbf{p}g^{\ddagger})]
to
\wp^{\gamma}=[(\gamma(x),\gamma\mathbf{p}g^{\ddagger}\gamma^{-1})]
, extends to a twisting construction between
1
-motives

(\mathcal{Q},\bm{\lambda},[u]_{(K_{\Phi})^{g^{\ddagger}}})\mapsto(\mathcal{Q}^{\gamma},\bm{\lambda}^{\gamma},[u^{\gamma}]_{(K_{\Phi})^{g^{\ddagger}\gamma^{-1}}}).

This action is compatible with twisting of abelian schemes on
\mathcal{S}_{K}
constructed by Kisin
[
36
]
and Kisin-Pappas
[
33
]
, under the isomorphism of formal completions in
[
50
, Thm. 4.1.5 (5)]
for Hodge-type Shimura varieties.

With this, we show that the integral model of the boundary mixed Shimura variety associated with
ZP_{\Phi}
for any cusp label representative
\Phi
of
(G,X_{a})
is a torus torsor when
K_{p}\cap Z_{G}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
contains the parahoric subgroup of
Z_{G}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
(see Lemma
4.25
, Lemma
4.26
and Proposition
4.30
). Then we extend this result to arbitrary level structures essentially by using an explicit description of the torus part of the Siegel-type boundary mixed Shimura varieties (see §
4.3.2
).

Organization of the paper

In Section
1
, we review Pink’s characteristic
0
theory for canonical models of toroidal compactifications of mixed Shimura varieties. Subsequently, we introduce a definition of coarser cusp labels, which helps us with finding compatible choices of cone decompositions for
(G_{0},X_{0})
and
(G_{2},X_{2})
. This selection of cusp labels and cones also enables us to construct
\mathcal{S}_{K_{2}}^{\Sigma_{2}}
as the pullback of some quotient in later steps. Some group-theoretic lemmas are established along the way.

In Section
2
, the main goal is to review the theory of degeneration of abelian schemes developed in
[
59
]
,
[
21
]
and
[
44
]
. The main difference is that we review it in the language of
1
-motives (cf.
[
72
]
and
[
50
]
); some propositions are recorded for later use.

In Section
3
, we extend Kisin-Pappas’ construction of twisting abelian schemes up to prime-to-
p
isogenies to the twisting of
1
-motives and semiabelian schemes with additional structures. We then explain that the twisting constructions on the interior
\mathcal{S}_{K_{0}}
and on the boundaries are compatible and induce an action on toroidal compactifications.

In Section
4
, we first generalize Deligne’s induction to construct boundary mixed Shimura varieties associated with
\mathrm{Sh}^{\Sigma_{2}^{\prime}}_{K_{2}}
. We then construct
\mathcal{S}_{K_{2}}^{\Sigma_{2}^{\prime}}
as the “subquotients” described above and present the main theorem (see Theorem
4.39
).

To complete the construction of a good compactification theory, we also construct the minimal compactification
\mathcal{S}_{K_{2}}^{\mathrm{min}}
of
\mathcal{S}_{K_{2}}
. We finish the paper by proving Pink’s formula and by showing that the adjunction morphisms of nearby cycles are isomorphisms (see Section
5
).

§
1.4
, §
3.3
, §
3.4
, §
4.2
and §
4.3
are the main technical subsections of this paper.

A list of symbols appearing in the first and the second sections is attached as an appendix for the convenience of the readers.

Here is a diagram showing the rough relationships among all sections and many key references in the literature. We apologize to the readers that we are not able to exhibit all the references we have used or to describe all the details within one diagram for now.

]

]

]

[

]

]
&
[

]

]

]
,
[

]
,
[

]

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces+App.\ref{cpr-pel-cl}\text{ compatible}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lx@sectionsign 1.4}

Notation and Conventions

General notation

•

In this article, all rings have identities. We fix a prime number
p
.

•

Let
\square
be either
\{p\}
or
\emptyset
. For example, if
\square=\emptyset
,
\mathbb{Z}_{(\square)}=\mathbb{Q}
,
\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{\square}=\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}=\varprojlim_{n\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}}\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}
, and
\mathbb{A}_{f}^{\square}=\mathbb{A}_{f}
, the finite adeles over
\mathbb{Q}
; if
\square=\{p\}
,
\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{\square}:=\varprojlim_{n\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}\text{ and }p\nmid n}\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}
and
\mathbb{A}_{f}^{\square}=\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p}
the finite adeles away from
p
.

•

In the notation “
\otimes_{R}
” for tensor products over a commutative ring
R
, we will omit the subscript “
R
” when
R=\mathbb{Z}
, or when
R
is clear in the context.

•

(Conjugation)
Let
G
be a group acting on a set
S
. Let
S_{1}
be a subset of
S
. We denote the image in
S
of the action of
g\in G
on
S_{1}
by
{}^{g}{S_{1}}
(resp.
S_{1}^{g}
) if the action of
G
is a left action (resp. right action). In particular, let
K
be a subgroup of a group
G
and let
g\in G
be any element in
G
, then we write
{}^{g}{K}:=gKg^{-1}
and write
{K}^{g}:=g^{-1}Kg
.

•

Let
E
be a number field and let
v
be a place of
E
. We denote by
\mathcal{O}_{E,(v)}
the localization of
\mathcal{O}_{E}
at the prime corresponding to
v
and by
\mathcal{O}_{E,v}
its completion. We write the subscript of
E_{v}
out to avoid multiple subscripts in the notation.

•

Let
\mathcal{F}
be an fppf sheaf of
B
-modules on a scheme
S
. Let
A
be a
B
-algebra. Denote
\underline{\mathrm{Aut}}_{A}\mathcal{F}:=(\underline{\mathrm{End}}_{S}\mathcal{F}\otimes_{B}A)^{\times}
. Let
C
be an
A
-algebra, and let
T
be an object in
S_{fppf}
, we can form
\underline{\mathrm{Aut}}_{A}(\mathcal{F}(T))(C):=((\underline{\mathrm{End}}_{S}\mathcal{F})(T)\otimes_{B}C)^{\times}
, the group of
C
-valued automorphisms of
\mathcal{F}|_{T}
. To save the notation, we denote
\underline{\mathrm{Aut}}_{A}(\mathcal{F})(A)
by
\underline{\mathrm{Aut}}_{A}\mathcal{F}
, which is a sheaf of
A
-modules. We will omit
A
in the above notation if
A=\mathbb{Z}
.

Notation related to Shimura varieties and compactifications

•

For a connected reductive group
G
, we denote by
G^{\mathrm{der}}
the derived group (resp.
G^{\mathrm{ad}}
the adjoint group) of
G
. For an algebraic group
G
over
\mathbb{R}
or
\mathbb{C}
, denote by
G^{\circ}
the identity component of
G
. For a Lie group
\mathscr{H}
over
\mathbb{R}
or
\mathbb{C}
, denote by
\mathscr{H}^{+}
its identity component. So it makes sense to write
G(\mathbb{R})^{+}
or
G(\mathbb{C})^{+}
. We will also use the symbol
G(\mathbb{R})_{+}
to denote the pullback to
G(\mathbb{R})
of the identity component
G^{\mathrm{ad}}(\mathbb{R})^{+}
under the natural homomorphism
G(\mathbb{R})\to G^{\mathrm{ad}}(\mathbb{R})
. Let
G(\mathbb{Q})?:=G(\mathbb{R})?\cap G(\mathbb{Q})
, where
?={}^{+},{}_{+}
. Let
G^{\mathrm{ad}}(\mathbb{R})_{1}
be the subgroup of
G^{\mathrm{ad}}(\mathbb{R})
that stabilizes
X\hookrightarrow X^{\mathrm{ad}}
, that is, the image of
G(\mathbb{R})
in
G^{\mathrm{ad}}(\mathbb{R})
. Define
G^{\mathrm{ad}}(\mathbb{Q})_{1}:=G^{\mathrm{ad}}(\mathbb{R})_{1}\cap G^{\mathrm{ad}}(\mathbb{Q})
, etc.

•

(Sign conventions)
We follow Pink’s sign conventions for Hodge and weight cocharacters (see
[
64
, Ch. 1]
), and the class field theory isomorphism (see
[
64
, 11.3]
).
The Hodge cocharacter
\mu_{\mathbb{C}}
is defined such that
z=(z,1)\in\mathbb{G}_{m}(\mathbb{C})\subset\mathbb{S}(\mathbb{C})
acts on
V^{-1,0}_{\mathbb{C}}
by
z
. The split torus
\mathbb{G}_{m,\mathbb{R}}
is embedded into
\mathbb{S}
by
i_{w}:\mathbb{G}_{m,\mathbb{R}}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{S}
,
r\in\mathbb{R}^{\times}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}^{\times}
. Let
V_{\mathbb{R}}
be an
\mathbb{R}
-vector space. Suppose there is a map
h:\mathbb{S}\to\mathrm{GL}(V_{\mathbb{R}})
. Define the weight cocharacter
\omega
to be
\omega:\mathbb{G}_{m,\mathbb{R}}\xrightarrow{i_{w}}\mathbb{S}\to\mathrm{GL}(V_{\mathbb{R}})
. We say that
h
has weight
-n
if
\omega(r)
sends
v\in V_{\mathbb{R}}
to
r^{n}v
.
Moreover, we choose the class field theory isomorphism
\mathrm{rec}_{E}:\mathrm{Gal}(E^{ab}/E)\cong\pi_{0}(\mathbb{G}_{m}(\mathbb{A}_{E})/\mathbb{G}_{m}(E))
such that, for any non-archimedean place
v
of
E_{v}
with a uniformizer
\pi_{v}
, and any finite abelian field extension
L/E
such that
L/E
is unramified at
v
,
(\cdots,\pi_{v}^{-1},\cdots)
corresponds to (arithmetic) Frobenius of
\mathrm{Gal}(L/E)
.

•

(Subgroups)
Let
G
be a linear algebraic group over
\mathbb{Q}
. A subgroup
\Gamma
in
G(\mathbb{Q})
is called a congruence subgroup if it is of the form
\Gamma=G(\mathbb{Q})\cap K
for some open compact subgroup
K\subset G(\mathbb{A}_{f})
. A subgroup
\Gamma^{\prime}
of
G(\mathbb{Q})
is called an arithmetic subgroup if it is commensurable with (any congruence subgroup)
\Gamma
.

Assume that
G
is connected reductive. We call an open compact subgroup
K_{p}
of
G(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
a Bruhat-Tits stabilizer subgroup if
K_{p}
is the group of
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-points of a Bruhat-Tits stabilizer group scheme
\mathscr{G}
. Denote by
K_{p}^{\circ}
the parahoric subgroup associated with
K_{p}
, i.e., the
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-points of the parahoric group scheme
\mathscr{G}^{\circ}
associated with
\mathscr{G}
. An open compact subgroup
K_{p}^{\prime\prime}\subset G(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
is called a quasi-parahoric subgroup (associated with
K_{p}
) if
K_{p}^{\circ}\subset K_{p}^{\prime\prime}\subset K_{p}
.

•

(Neatness)
Let
G
be a linear algebraic group over
\mathbb{Q}
. Let
\square=\{p\},
or
\emptyset
. Following Pink’s convention, we say an open compact subgroup
K^{\square}\subset G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{\square})
is
neat
if, for some (and for any) faithful representation
i:G\hookrightarrow\mathrm{GL}(V)
and any
g=(g_{\ell})_{\ell\notin\square}\in K^{\square}
, the intersection

\bigcap\limits_{\ell\notin\square}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}^{\times}\cap\Gamma_{\ell})_{\mathrm{torsion}}

is trivial. In the intersection above,
\Gamma_{\ell}
is defined to be the group generated by the eigenvalues of
g_{\ell}
in
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}^{\times}
.

If
K^{\square}
is neat, then for any
g\in G(\mathbb{A}_{f})
and any open compact subgroup
K\subset G(\mathbb{A}_{f})
such that the projection of
K
to
G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{\square})
is
K^{\square}
, the intersection
gKg^{-1}\cap G(\mathbb{Q})
is a neat (congruence) subgroup. See
[
64
, pp.12-13]
.
Although neatness will be assumed throughout the paper, we will re-emphasized it when this assumption is crucial in the argument.

•

(
Extension property
) Let
\mathcal{O}
be a Dedekind domain with fraction field
K
and let
X
be an
\mathcal{O}
-scheme. Following
[
36
]
, we say that
X
has the extension property if, for any regular and formally smooth
\mathcal{O}
-scheme
S
, any morphism
S\otimes_{\mathcal{O}}K\to X
extends to a morphism
S\to X
.

•

(
Cones
) Let
\mathbf{E}\cong\mathbb{G}_{m}^{n}
be a split algebraic torus over
\mathbb{Z}
. The rational polyhedral cones in this paper are
closed
, i.e., a rational polyhedral cone
\sigma
in the cocharacter group
X_{*}(\mathbf{E})_{\mathbb{R}}
is a subset determined by
\sigma=\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}v_{1}+\cdots+\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}v_{m}
for
v_{i}\in X_{*}(\mathbf{E})_{\mathbb{Q}}
and
1\leq i\leq m\leq n
. This convention coincides with those in
[
64
]
and
[
50
]
. The
\sigma
in
[
44
, Def. 6.1.1.5]
is written as
\sigma^{\circ}
in this paper. Here,
\sigma^{\circ}
denotes the interior of the closed cone
\sigma
. The closure
\overline{\sigma}
in
loc. cit.
is written as
\sigma
in this paper.

Let
\mathcal{T}
be an
\mathbf{E}
-torsor over a scheme
\mathcal{S}
. Denote by
\mathcal{T}(\sigma)
the twisted (relatively) affine torus embedding and by
\mathcal{T}_{\sigma}
the
\sigma
-stratum (see
[
64
, 5.2]
,
[
44
, Sec. 6.1.2]
and
[
50
, 2.1.17]
). These two terms defined in
[
64
, 5.2]
and
[
44
, Sec. 6.1.2]
are the same because the definitions of
\sigma^{\vee}
and
\sigma^{\perp}
remain the same for the two different definitions of
\sigma
.

•

When writing an embedding of (integral models of) Shimura varieties, we often omit the reflex fields. That is,
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{1}}(G_{1},X_{1})\hookrightarrow\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{2}}(G_{2},X_{2})
means
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{1}}(G_{1},X_{1})\hookrightarrow\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{2}}(G_{2},X_{2})\otimes_{E(G_{2},X_{2})}E(G_{1},X_{1})
. We will also omit the reflex fields when saying relative normalizations. That is, the relative normalization in
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{1}}(G_{1},X_{1})
of
\mathcal{S}_{K_{2}}(G_{2},X_{2})
in fact means the relative normalization in
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{1}}(G_{1},X_{1})
of
\mathcal{S}_{K_{2}}(G_{2},X_{2})_{\mathcal{O}_{E_{1}(G_{1},X_{1}),(v_{1})}}
or
\mathcal{S}_{K_{2}}(G_{2},X_{2})_{\mathcal{O}_{E_{1}(G_{1},X_{1}),v_{1}}}
.

1.
Characteristic zero theory

This section has two main goals. The first one (see §
1.1
and §
1.3
) is to review and summarize some key definitions and theorems in Pink’s thesis
[
64
]
. See also a summary of it in
[
50
, Sec. 2.1]
. The second one (see §
1.4
) is a preparation for our main construction in Section
4
. Namely, we shall introduce our strategy of passing from Hodge-type Shimura data to abelian-type ones, and we shall explain how to make a good choice of cone decompositions.

1.1.
Boundary charts in characteristic zero theory

In §
1.1
, we collect some definitions and results in
[
64
]
and
[
50
, Sec. 2.1]
due to Pink and Madapusi.

1.1.1.

Let
P
be a connected linear algebraic group over
\mathbb{Q}
. Let
W
be the unipotent radical of
P
and let
U
be a normal subgroup of
W
. Let
\mathcal{X}
be a
P(\mathbb{R})U(\mathbb{C})
-homogeneous space together with a
P(\mathbb{R})U(\mathbb{C})
-equivariant morphism
??:\mathcal{X}\to\mathrm{Hom}(\mathbb{S}_{\mathbb{C}},P_{\mathbb{C}})
. The triple
(P,\mathcal{X},\hbar)
is called a
mixed Shimura datum
if it satisfies the conditions listed in
[
64
, Def. 2.1]
. We will omit the morphism
\hbar
in the notation of a mixed Shimura datum when
\hbar
is clear in the context. We denote
\hbar(P,\mathcal{X}):=(P,\hbar(\mathcal{X}))
.

When
W=1
, the triple is called a
pure Shimura datum
. We do not omit the word “pure” to distinguish it from the usual definition of Shimura data as in
[
17
]
,
[
20
]
and so on.
In other words, if
(P,\mathcal{X},\hbar)
is a pure Shimura datum,
\hbar(P,\mathcal{X})
is a Shimura datum in the sense of
loc. cit
., but
(P,\mathcal{X},\hbar)
itself is a Shimura datum if and only if
\hbar
is a homeomorphism.

Let
P^{\prime}
be a normal
\mathbb{Q}
-subgroup of
P
. Define
T:=P/P^{\prime}
with a canonical quotient map
\phi:P\to T
. As in
[
64
, Prop. 2.9]
, there is a canonical
quotient mixed Shimura datum

(T,\mathcal{Y})
of
(P,\mathcal{X})
by
P^{\prime}
, and a canonical
quotient morphism
, which we abusively denote by
\phi:(P,\mathcal{X})\to(T,\mathcal{Y})
.
In fact, there is an isomorphism
\mathcal{Y}\cong T(\mathbb{R})\phi(U(\mathbb{C}))/\phi(\mathrm{Stab}_{P(\mathbb{R})U(\mathbb{C})}(x))
for any
x\in\mathcal{X}
. Sometimes we will abusively write
\mathcal{Y}
as
\mathcal{X}/P^{\prime}
, although
\mathcal{Y}
is not a quotient of
\mathcal{X}
in general. In particular, if
T
is a torus,
(T,\mathcal{Y})
being a mixed Shimura datum implies that
\mathcal{Y}
is finite and discrete. Hence,
\phi(\mathrm{Stab}_{P(\mathbb{R})U(\mathbb{C})}(x))
is a subgroup of
T(\mathbb{R})\phi(U(\mathbb{C}))
containing
(T(\mathbb{R})\phi(U(\mathbb{C})))^{+}
.

A mixed Shimura datum
(P,\mathcal{X},\hbar)
is
irreducible
if
P
is the only normal
\mathbb{Q}
-subgroup
Q
of
P
such that all homomorphisms
h_{x}:\mathbb{S}_{\mathbb{C}}\to P_{\mathbb{C}}
defined by
x\in\hbar(\mathcal{X})
factor through
Q_{\mathbb{C}}
.

1.1.2.

Let
(P,\mathcal{X})
be a mixed Shimura datum. For any neat open compact subgroup
K
in
P(\mathbb{A}_{f})
, define

\mathrm{Sh}_{K}(P,\mathcal{X})(\mathbb{C}):=P(\mathbb{Q})\backslash\mathcal{X}\times P(\mathbb{A}_{f})/K.

The group
P(\mathbb{Q})
acts on
\mathcal{X}\times P(\mathbb{A}_{f})
diagonally on the left, while the group
K
acts only on
P(\mathbb{A}_{f})
on the right.
By
[
64
, Prop. 3.3 (b)]
, the double coset
\mathrm{Sh}_{K}(P,\mathcal{X})(\mathbb{C})
is a complex manifold; by
[
64
, Prop. 6.26 and Prop. 9.24]
, it admits a canonical structure of a smooth quasi-projective variety
\mathrm{Sh}_{K}(P,\mathcal{X})_{\mathbb{C}}
over
\mathbb{C}
.

Let
p_{f}\in P(\mathbb{A}_{f})
and choose
K^{\prime}
such that
p_{f}^{-1}K^{\prime}p_{f}\subset K
, we can define a morphism

\pi_{K^{\prime},K}(p_{f}):\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{\prime}}(P,\mathcal{X})(\mathbb{C})\longrightarrow\mathrm{Sh}_{K}(P,\mathcal{X})(\mathbb{C})

sending
[(x,p)]
to
[(x,p\cdot p_{f})]
. Then the map
\pi_{K^{\prime},K}(p_{f})
also algebraizes to a morphism between mixed Shimura varieties. Any morphism between mixed Shimura data
f:(P_{1},\mathcal{X}_{1})\to(P_{2},\mathcal{X}_{2})
, with a compatible choice of neat open compact subgroups
K_{1}\subset P_{1}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
and
K_{2}\subset P_{2}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
such that
K_{1}\subset K_{2}
, also induces a morphism between mixed Shimura varieties
f:\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{1}}(P_{1},\mathcal{X}_{1})_{\mathbb{C}}\to\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{2}}(P_{2},\mathcal{X}_{2})_{\mathbb{C}}
.

Let us briefly recall the definition of canonical models of mixed Shimura varieties (see
[
64
, Ch. 11]
and also
[
34
, 1.5.3]
).
If the mixed Shimura datum is of the form
(T,\mathcal{Y})
where
T
is a torus and
\mathcal{Y}
is a finite set, there is a morphism

R_{E_{T}/\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{G}_{m,E_{T}}\xrightarrow{R_{E_{T}/\mathbb{Q}}\mu_{y}}R_{E_{T}/\mathbb{Q}}T_{E_{T}}\xrightarrow{\mathrm{Norm}_{E_{T}/\mathbb{Q}}}T,

where
E_{T}=E(T,\mathcal{Y})
and
\mu_{y}
is the Hodge cocharacter associated with the homomorphism
h_{y}:\mathbb{S}_{\mathbb{C}}\to T_{\mathbb{C}}
determined by any
y\in\mathcal{Y}
. This is independent of the choice of
y
since
\hbar(\mathcal{Y})
is a single point.
The morphism above induces a continuous homomorphism
\pi_{0}(\mathbb{G}_{m}(\mathbb{A}_{E_{T}})/\mathbb{G}_{m}(E_{T}))\to\pi_{0}(T(\mathbb{A})/T(\mathbb{Q}))
. Pre-composing this homomorphism with the reciprocity law
\mathrm{rec}_{E_{T}}:\mathrm{Gal}(E^{ab}_{T}/E_{T})\xrightarrow{\sim}\pi_{0}(\mathbb{G}_{m}(\mathbb{A}_{E_{T}})/\mathbb{G}_{m}(E_{T}))
, we obtain

r_{E_{T}}(T,\mathcal{Y}):\mathrm{Gal}(\overline{E}_{T}/E_{T})\longrightarrow\mathrm{Gal}(E^{ab}_{T}/E_{T})\longrightarrow\pi_{0}(T(\mathbb{A})/T(\mathbb{Q})).

Let
K_{T}
be any neat open compact subgroup in
T(\mathbb{A}_{f})
, since
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{T}}(T,\mathcal{Y})(\overline{E}_{T})=T(\mathbb{Q})\backslash\mathcal{Y}\times T(\mathbb{A}_{f})/K_{T}
is a finite set, the left action of
T(\mathbb{A})/T(\mathbb{Q})
on it factors through
\pi_{0}(T(\mathbb{A})/T(\mathbb{Q}))
. Hence, there is a well-defined left action of
\mathrm{Gal}(\overline{E}_{T}/E_{T})
on
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{T}}(T,\mathcal{Y})(\overline{E}_{T})
through
r_{E_{T}}(T,\mathcal{Y})
. This action forms a descent datum, which determines a canonical model
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{T}}(T,\mathcal{Y})
for
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{T}}(T,\mathcal{Y})_{\mathbb{C}}
over
E_{T}
.

By
[
64
, Thm. 11.18]
, for every mixed Shimura datum
(P,\mathcal{X})
and
K
neat open compact subgroup of
P(\mathbb{A}_{f})
,
\mathrm{Sh}_{K}(P,\mathcal{X})_{\mathbb{C}}
admits a
canonical model

\mathrm{Sh}_{K}(P,\mathcal{X})
over its reflex field
E:=E(P,\mathcal{X})
, and this canonical model is uniquely characterized by the following two properties:

•

For any
p_{f}
and
K^{\prime}
as above, the morphism
\pi_{K^{\prime},K}(p_{f})
descends to
E
;

•

For any embedding
\iota:(T,\mathcal{Y})\hookrightarrow(P,\mathcal{X})
and any
\iota(K_{T})\subset K
, the induced morphism
\iota:\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{T}}(T,\mathcal{Y})_{\mathbb{C}}\to\mathrm{Sh}_{K}(P,\mathcal{X})_{\mathbb{C}}
descends to
E_{T}
.

1.1.3.

Let
G
be a connected reductive group over
\mathbb{Q}
and
X
be a
G(\mathbb{R})
-conjugacy class of homomorphisms of the form
h:\mathbb{S}\to G_{\mathbb{R}}
. Let
(G,X)
be a Shimura datum, which means that
(G,X)
satisfies
[
20
, 2.1.1.1-2.1.1.3]
. We fix a triple
(H_{0},h_{0},h_{\infty})
as in
[
64
, Sec. 4.3]
. More precisely, let
??
be the reference group defined as in
loc. cit.
over
\mathbb{R}
. In fact,
H_{0}
is characterized by

H_{0}(\mathbb{R}):=\{(z,\alpha)\in\mathbb{S}(\mathbb{R})\times\mathrm{GL}_{2}(\mathbb{R})||z|^{2}=\det\alpha\},\text{ and}

H_{0}(\mathbb{C}):=\{((z_{1},z_{2});\alpha)\in\mathbb{S}(\mathbb{C})\times\mathrm{GL}_{2}(\mathbb{C})|z_{1}z_{2}=\det\alpha\}.

Let
h_{0}:\mathbb{S}\to H_{0}
and
h_{\infty}:\mathbb{S}\to H_{0}
be homomorphisms from
\mathbb{S}
to
H_{0}
chosen as in
loc. cit.
, with associated weight cocharacters
\omega_{0}
and
\omega_{\infty}
obtained by pre-composing with the natural embedding
\mathbb{G}_{m,\mathbb{R}}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{S}
.

Let us recall the definition of
admissible
\mathbb{Q}
-parabolic subgroups
(see
[
64
, Def. 4.5]
). The adjoint group
G^{\mathrm{ad}}
of
G
can be decomposed as a product
G^{\mathrm{ad}}=\prod_{i=1}^{k}G_{i}
of
\mathbb{Q}
-simple reductive groups
G_{i}
. An admissible
\mathbb{Q}
-parabolic subgroup
Q
is the preimage in
G
of a subgroup
Q^{\mathrm{ad}}=\prod_{i=1}^{k}Q_{i}
, where
Q_{i}\subset G_{i}
is either
G_{i}
or a maximal proper
\mathbb{Q}
-parabolic subgroup of
G_{i}
.

Let
Q
be any admissible
\mathbb{Q}
-parabolic subgroup of
G
. Then the homomorphisms
h_{0}
and
h_{\infty}
are chosen such that
\omega_{0}\cdot\omega_{\infty}^{-1}
factors through
H_{0}^{\mathrm{der}}
. The difference
\lambda_{H_{0}}:=\omega_{\infty}\cdot\omega_{0}^{-1}
is a cocharacter
\lambda_{H_{0}}:\mathbb{G}_{m,\mathbb{R}}\to H_{0}
. Let
B_{0}
(resp.
U_{0}
) be the Borel subgroup (resp. unipotent radical of a Borel subgroup) of
H_{0}
corresponding to the subspace of nonnegative (resp. positive) weights under the adjoint representation of
\lambda_{H_{0}}
on
\lie H_{0}
.
For any admissible
\mathbb{Q}
-parabolic subgroup
Q
of
G
and any
x\in X
, there is a unique homomorphism
??:H_{0,\mathbb{C}}\to G_{\mathbb{C}}
characterized by the conditions listed in
[
64
, Prop. 4.6]
such that
u_{x}^{Q}\circ h_{0}=h_{x}
, and such that
u_{x}^{Q}\circ h_{\infty}
and
\omega_{x,\infty}^{Q}:=u_{x}^{Q}\circ\omega_{\infty}
factor through
Q_{\mathbb{C}}
. Let
\omega_{x,0}^{Q}:=u_{x}^{Q}\circ\omega_{0}=\omega_{x}
. Then
\omega_{x,0}^{Q}\cdot\omega_{x,\infty}^{Q,-1}
factors through
G_{\mathbb{C}}^{\mathrm{der}}
.

A
cusp label representative

??
of
(G,X)
is defined by a triple
\Phi=(Q_{\Phi},X^{+}_{\Phi},g_{\Phi})
as in
[
50
, 2.1.7]
, where
Q_{\Phi}
is an admissible
\mathbb{Q}
-parabolic subgroup of
G
,
X^{+}_{\Phi}
is a connected component of
X
and
g_{\Phi}
is an element in
G(\mathbb{A}_{f})
. For any
x\in X
, we can denote by
[x]
its image under
X\to\pi_{0}(X)
, which represents a connected component of
X
.
Let
??
be the smallest (connected) normal
\mathbb{Q}
-subgroup of
??
, such that
u^{Q_{\Phi}}_{x}\circ h_{\infty}
factors through
P_{\Phi,\mathbb{C}}
. We can also denote
P_{\Phi}
by
P_{Q_{\Phi}}
because it only depends on
Q_{\Phi}
.

Let
W_{\Phi}
be
the unipotent radical of
Q_{\Phi}
and let
??
be the center of
??
(see
[
5
, III. Cor. 4.4, p.147]
).
Consider the
Q_{\Phi}(\mathbb{R})U_{\Phi}(\mathbb{C})
-equivariant map

(1.1)

??:X\longrightarrow\pi_{0}(X)\times\mathrm{Hom}(\mathbb{S}_{\mathbb{C}},P_{\Phi,\mathbb{C}})

defined by mapping
x\in X
to
([x],u_{x}^{Q_{\Phi}}\circ h_{\infty})
. By
[
64
, Sec. 4.11]
, the
P_{\Phi}(\mathbb{R})U_{\Phi}(\mathbb{C})
-orbit of the image of any
x\in X
under the map above only depends on the image of
x
in
\pi_{0}(X)
.

Let
??
be the
P_{\Phi}(\mathbb{R})U_{\Phi}(\mathbb{C})
-orbit of the image of
x_{\Phi}\in X^{+}_{\Phi}
under (
1.1
). The morphism over connected components
\pi_{0}(\tau^{-1}(D_{\Phi}))\xrightarrow{\sim}\pi_{0}(D_{\Phi})
induced by
\tau
is a bijection. We can also denote it by
D_{Q_{\Phi},X^{+}_{\Phi}}
, because it only depends on
Q_{\Phi}
and
X^{+}_{\Phi}
.

By
[
64
, Sec. 4.11]
, the pair
??
is a mixed Shimura datum, which is called the
rational boundary component of
(G,X)
associated with
\Phi
. Define
\overline{P}_{\Phi}:=P_{\Phi}/U_{\Phi}
and
P_{\Phi,h}:=P_{\Phi}/W_{\Phi}
. Then we have mixed Shimura data defined by
(\overline{P}_{\Phi},\overline{D}_{\Phi}):=(P_{\Phi},D_{\Phi})/U_{\Phi}
and
(P_{\Phi,h},D_{\Phi,h}):=(P_{\Phi},D_{\Phi})/W_{\Phi}
as in
[
64
, Prop. 2.9]
. Note that
(P_{\Phi,h},D_{\Phi,h})
is a pure Shimura datum.

For any neat open compact subgroup
K
of
G(\mathbb{A}_{f})
, define
K_{\Phi}:=g_{\Phi}K{g_{\Phi}}^{-1}\cap P_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f}).
Define
\overline{K}_{\Phi}
(resp.
K_{\Phi,h}
) to be the image of
K_{\Phi}
in
\overline{P}_{\Phi}
(resp.
P_{\Phi,h}
) via the obvious quotient maps.
Define double coset spaces
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi}}(P_{\Phi},D_{\Phi})(\mathbb{C})

:=P_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})\backslash D_{\Phi}\times P_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f})/K_{\Phi}
,
\mathrm{Sh}_{\overline{K}_{\Phi}}(\overline{P}_{\Phi},\overline{D}_{\Phi})(\mathbb{C}):=\overline{P}_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})\backslash\overline{D}_{\Phi}\times\overline{P}_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f})/\overline{K}_{\Phi}
and
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi,h}}({P}_{\Phi,h},{D}_{\Phi,h})(\mathbb{C}):={P}_{\Phi,h}(\mathbb{Q})\backslash D_{\Phi,h}\times{P}_{\Phi,h}(\mathbb{A}_{f})/{K}_{\Phi,h}
, as in
[
50
, 2.1.7]
. They admit canonical structures of smooth quasi-projective algebraic varieties over
\mathbb{C}
and they admit canonical models over the reflex field
E:=E(G,X)
associated with
(G,X)
(see §
1.1.2
above). Denote their canonical models by
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi}}(P_{\Phi},D_{\Phi})
,
\mathrm{Sh}_{\overline{K}_{\Phi}}(\overline{P}_{\Phi},\overline{D}_{\Phi})
and
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi,h}}(P_{\Phi,h},D_{\Phi,h})
, respectively. There is a tower
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi}}\to\overline{\mathrm{Sh}}_{K_{\Phi}}\to\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi},h}
over
\spec E
.

We call
(P_{\Phi},D_{\Phi})
(resp.
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi}}(P_{\Phi},D_{\Phi})
) the
boundary mixed Shimura datum
(resp.
boundary mixed Shimura variety
) associated with
(G,X;\Phi)
(resp.
(G,X;\Phi;K)
).

Remark 1.1
.

Note that our system of notation is slightly different from those in
[
64
]
and
[
50
]
, and the systems of notation in the just-mentioned references are also slightly different. See a brief comparison in the List of Symbols.

Convention 1.2
.

To avoid overloaded notation, we will frequently adopt the following abbreviations:
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi}}:=\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi}}(P_{\Phi},D_{\Phi})
,
\overline{\mathrm{Sh}}_{K_{\Phi}}:=\mathrm{Sh}_{\overline{K}_{\Phi}}(\overline{P}_{\Phi},\overline{D}_{\Phi})
and
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi},h}:=\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi,h}}(P_{\Phi,h},D_{\Phi,h})
; similarly, we write
??
,
??
and
??
for the base change to a field extension
F
of
E=E(G,X)
; we write
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi}}(F)
,
\overline{\mathrm{Sh}}_{K_{\Phi}}(F)
and
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi},h}(F)
for
F
-valued points. In other words, we will frequently omit the data in the brackets, which is usually harmless, since the information is hidden in the given data
\Phi
and the sub/super-scripts of the level groups.

Since
D_{Q_{\Phi},X_{\Phi}^{+}}
is a
P_{\Phi}(\mathbb{R})U_{\Phi}(\mathbb{C})
-orbit, by
[
64
, 4.14]
, there is a natural continuous map
\im:D_{Q_{\Phi},X_{\Phi}^{+}}\to U_{\Phi}(\mathbb{R})(-1):=(2\pi\sqrt{-1})^{-1}U_{\Phi}(\mathbb{R})
, mapping
x\in D_{Q_{\Phi},X_{\Phi}^{+}}
to its imaginary part
u_{x}\in U_{\Phi}(\mathbb{R})(-1)
. As explained in
[
64
, 4.15]
, one associates with
(Q_{\Phi},X_{\Phi}^{+})
an open homogeneous self-adjoint non-degenerate cone
??
in the sense of
[
5
, Ch. II]
.

1.1.4.

Let
??
be the set of all cusp label representatives for
(G,X)
defined as in §
1.1.3
.
Note that
G(\mathbb{Q})
acts on
\mathcal{CLR}(G,X)
by sending
\Phi
to

\gamma\Phi:=(\gamma Q_{\Phi}\gamma^{-1},\gamma X^{+}_{\Phi},\gamma g_{\Phi})

for any
\gamma\in G(\mathbb{Q})
.

More importantly, we can also view the action of
\gamma
as the one induced by the conjugation

\gamma:G\to G;\ g\mapsto\gamma g\gamma^{-1}.

This is a homomorphism between algebraic groups. This homomorphism induces a morphism between Shimura data and Shimura varieties

\gamma:(G,X)\to(G,X);\ \mathrm{Sh}_{K}(G,X)\to\mathrm{Sh}_{{}^{\gamma}{K}}(G,X).

By
[
64
, 4.16]
, this induces an automorphism of
\mathcal{CLR}(G,X)
, sending
\Phi
to a cusp label representative on the
target

\gamma\Phi:=(\gamma Q_{\Phi}\gamma^{-1},\gamma X_{\Phi}^{+},\gamma g_{\Phi}\gamma^{-1})
.

For both viewpoints, they induce the same morphism between mixed Shimura varieties. The second viewpoint is more useful since it can be generalized to any conjugation of
\gamma\in G^{\mathrm{ad}}(\mathbb{Q})
.

Indeed, any
\gamma\in G^{\mathrm{ad}}(\mathbb{Q})
induces a conjugation of
G
, and
\gamma
acts on
X^{\mathrm{ad}}
, so it sends
X
to
\gamma\cdot X
viewing
X
as a subspace of
X^{\mathrm{ad}}
. Then
\gamma
induces a morphism between Shimura varieties,

\gamma:\mathrm{Sh}_{K}(G,X)\to\mathrm{Sh}_{{}^{\gamma}{K}}(G,\gamma\cdot X),

sending
[(x,g)]
to
[\gamma\cdot x,\gamma g\gamma^{-1}]
.
Then we can define the action of
G^{\mathrm{ad}}(\mathbb{Q})
on
\mathcal{CLR}(G,X)
, i.e., defining
\gamma\Phi:=(\gamma Q_{\Phi}\gamma^{-1},\gamma X_{\Phi}^{+},\gamma g_{\Phi}\gamma^{-1})
.

Moreover,
G(\mathbb{A}_{f})
, and in particular,
P_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
and
K
, can act on
\mathcal{CLR}(G,X)
on both left and right: if we write them as left actions, then
(g_{1},g_{2})\cdot\Phi:=(Q_{\Phi},X^{+}_{\Phi},g_{1}g_{\Phi}g_{2}^{-1})
for any
g_{1},g_{2}\in G(\mathbb{A}_{f})
.

Let
\Phi_{1}=(Q_{\Phi_{1}},X^{+}_{\Phi_{1}},g_{\Phi_{1}})
and
\Phi_{2}=(Q_{\Phi_{2}},X^{+}_{\Phi_{2}},g_{\Phi_{2}})
be two cusp label representatives.
As
[
50
, 2.1.14]
, we say
\Phi_{1}
and
\Phi_{2}
are
equivalent
if there is a
\gamma\in G(\mathbb{Q})
such that
\gamma Q_{\Phi_{1}}\gamma^{-1}=Q_{\Phi_{2}}
,
P_{\Phi_{1}}(\mathbb{Q})\gamma X^{+}_{\Phi_{1}}=P_{\Phi_{2}}(\mathbb{Q})X^{+}_{\Phi_{2}}
, and there is a
q\in P_{\Phi_{2}}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
such that
\gamma g_{\Phi_{1}}\equiv qg_{\Phi_{2}}
modulo
K
; we denote this equivalence by

\Phi_{1}\xrightarrow[\sim]{(\gamma,q)_{K}}\Phi_{2}.

An equivalent interpretation of the last condition is that
\gamma g_{\Phi_{1}}
and
g_{\Phi_{2}}
have the same image in
P_{\Phi_{2}}(\mathbb{A}_{f})\backslash G(\mathbb{A}_{f})/K
.
The notion of equivalence depends on the open compact group
K
. Denote by
??
the set of equivalence classes of cusp label representatives, and the elements
[\Phi]
in it are called
cusp labels
.
We also write
\Phi_{1}\xrightarrow{(\gamma,q)_{K}}\Phi_{2}
if
\gamma P_{\Phi_{1}}\gamma^{-1}\subset P_{\Phi_{2}}
,
P_{\Phi_{1}}(\mathbb{Q})\gamma X_{\Phi_{1}}^{+}=P_{\Phi_{2}}(\mathbb{Q})X^{+}_{\Phi_{2}}
and
\gamma g_{\Phi_{1}}\equiv qg_{\Phi_{2}}
modulo
K
. Define a partial order
\preceq
on
\mathrm{Cusp}_{K}(G,X)
by writing
[\Phi_{1}]\preceq[\Phi_{2}]
if and only if
\Phi_{1}\xrightarrow{(\gamma,q)_{K}}\Phi_{2}
for some
\gamma
and
q
.

Lemma 1.3
.

Let
\Phi_{1}
and
\Phi_{2}
be two cusp label representatives. Suppose that
Q_{\Phi_{1}}=Q_{\Phi_{2}}=Q
. Then there is an element
q\in Q(\mathbb{Q})
such that
qX^{+}_{\Phi_{1}}=X^{+}_{\Phi_{2}}
.

Proof.

Since
Q(\mathbb{Q})G(\mathbb{Q})_{+}\subset G(\mathbb{Q})
and
G(\mathbb{Q})/G(\mathbb{Q})_{+}\cong G(\mathbb{R})/G(\mathbb{R})_{+}
by real approximation (see
[
20
, 2.1.2]
), it suffices to show
Q(\mathbb{R})G(\mathbb{R})_{+}=G(\mathbb{R})
. Note
Q
is a parabolic subgroup and
G(\mathbb{R})_{+}
contains
K_{\infty}:=\mathrm{Stab}_{G(\mathbb{R})}(x)
for some
x\in X^{+}
. Then the statement follows from the Iwasawa decomposition (see
[
66
, Thm. 3.9, p.131]
).
∎

Proposition 1.4

(cf.
[
64
, 6.3]
and
[
65
, p.221]
)
.

Fix an admissible
\mathbb{Q}
-parabolic subgroup
Q
and a connected component
X^{+}
of
X
. The set
??
defined as

\{[\Phi]\in\mathrm{Cusp}_{K}(G,X)|\Phi=(Q,X^{+}_{\Phi},g_{\Phi}),\ X^{+}_{\Phi}\subset X\text{ connected component, }g_{\Phi}\in G(\mathbb{A}_{f})\}

consisting of cusp labels in
\mathrm{Cusp}_{K}(G,X)
that have representatives of the form
\Phi=(Q,X^{+}_{\Phi},g_{\Phi})
is in bijection with

\mathrm{Stab}_{Q(\mathbb{Q})}(D_{Q,X^{+}})P_{Q}(\mathbb{A}_{f})\backslash G(\mathbb{A}_{f})/K.

Proof.

By the definition of cusp labels, there is a well-defined map from

(1.2)

\mathrm{Stab}_{Q(\mathbb{Q})}(D_{Q,X^{+}})P_{Q}(\mathbb{A}_{f})\backslash G(\mathbb{A}_{f})/K

to
\mathrm{Cusp}_{K}(G,X)
mapping
[g]
to
[(Q,X^{+},g)]
. In fact, we only have to show that
(Q,X^{+},g)\sim(Q,X^{+},q\cdot p\cdot g\cdot k)
for any
q\in\mathrm{Stab}_{Q(\mathbb{Q})}(D_{Q,X^{+}})
,
p\in P_{Q}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
and
k\in K
. It suffices to check that
(Q,X^{+},q\cdot p\cdot g\cdot k)\xrightarrow[\sim]{({q^{-1}},{p})_{K}}(Q,X^{+},g)
. Since
q^{-1}(Q,X^{+},q\cdot p\cdot g\cdot k)=(Q,q^{-1}X^{+},p\cdot g\cdot k)
and
p\cdot g\cdot k\equiv p\cdot g
mod
K
, it suffices to show that
P_{Q}(\mathbb{Q})q^{-1}X^{+}=P_{Q}(\mathbb{Q})X^{+}
. Since
\tau
induces a bijection over connected components, it suffices to show that
P_{Q}(\mathbb{Q})q^{-1}\tau(X^{+})=P_{Q}(\mathbb{Q})\tau(X^{+})=D_{Q,X^{+}}
, and this follows from the condition that
q\in\mathrm{Stab}_{Q(\mathbb{Q})}(D_{Q,X^{+}})
.

Then we check that this map is injective. If
[g]
and
[g^{\prime}]
map to the same element in
\mathrm{Cusp}_{K}(G,X)
, then
\gamma\cdot g=p\cdot g^{\prime}\cdot k
for some
\gamma\in Q(\mathbb{Q})=\mathrm{Stab}_{G(\mathbb{Q})}(Q(\mathbb{Q}))
,
p\in P_{Q}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
and
k\in K
. Moreover,
\gamma
stabilizes
X^{+}
, so
\gamma\in\mathrm{Stab}_{Q(\mathbb{Q})}(D_{Q,X^{+}})
.

Finally, we check that this map is surjective. For any cusp label
[\Phi]=[(Q,X^{+}_{\Phi},g_{\Phi})]
, there is a
q\in Q(\mathbb{Q})
such that
qX^{+}_{\Phi}=X^{+}
by Lemma
1.3
, so such an equivalence class is in the image of the map.
∎

1.2.
Boundary mixed Shimura varieties of Hodge type

1.2.1.

In this subsection, we show that
(P_{\Phi,h},D_{\Phi,h})
is a Shimura datum in the usual sense when
(G,X)
is a Shimura datum of Hodge type or in slightly more general cases. Our proof does not rely on any detailed computation of the groups such as
P_{\Phi}
and
Q_{\Phi}
.

Denote by
N:\mathbb{S}\to\mathbb{G}_{m,\mathbb{R}}
the morphism induced by taking the norm over
\mathbb{R}
-points. Then
N(\mathbb{S}(\mathbb{R}))=\mathbb{R}^{\times}_{+}=\mathbb{G}_{m}(\mathbb{R})^{+}.
As in
[
64
, Example 2.8]
, we can define the
0
-dimensional Siegel Shimura datum to be the pair
(\mathbb{G}_{m},\mathbb{H}^{\pm}_{0})
, where
\mathbb{H}^{\pm}_{0}
is the
2
-point set defined by the set of isomorphisms between
\mathbb{Z}
and
\mathbb{Z}(1)
. Let
(G_{0},X_{0})
be a Shimura datum of Hodge type with an embedding into a Siegel Shimura datum
\iota:(G_{0},X_{0})\hookrightarrow(G^{\ddagger},X^{\ddagger})
.

There is a canonical
similitude character

\nu:=
??
:(G^{\ddagger},X^{\ddagger}):=(\mathrm{GSp}(V,\psi),\mathbb{H}_{g}^{\pm})\to(\mathbb{G}_{m},\mathbb{H}_{0}^{\pm})
from any Siegel Shimura datum associated with a symplectic space
(V,\psi)
of dimension
2g>0
to
(\mathbb{G}_{m},\mathbb{H}_{0}^{\pm})
. In fact, for any
x\in\mathbb{H}_{g}^{\pm}
, the point
\nu(x)
is the isomorphism
\lambda
such that
\lambda\circ\psi
is a polarization with respect to the Hodge structure associated with
h_{x}
; the map, abusively denoted by
\nu:\mathrm{GSp}(V,\psi)\to\mathbb{G}_{m}
, is defined to be the quotient map of
\mathrm{GSp}(V,\psi)
by its derived group.
From the explanation in §
1.1.1
, the quotient
\nu:\mathrm{GSp}(V,\psi)\to\mathbb{G}_{m}
induces a quotient of
(\mathrm{GSp}(V,\psi),\mathbb{H}_{g}^{\pm})
by
\mathrm{Sp}(V,\psi)
.

Convention 1.5
.

We fix a choice of
\sqrt{-1}
, and therefore fix a choice of
\mathbb{Z}(1)\cong\mathbb{Z}
. We denote by
X^{{\ddagger},+}
the connected component of
X^{\ddagger}
such that
\psi
is a (
positive
) polarization with respect to
h_{x}
for one of (and therefore all of) the points
x\in X^{{\ddagger},+}
.

Fix any cusp label representative
\Phi^{\ddagger}
. Recall that there is a
Q_{\Phi^{\ddagger}}(\mathbb{R})
-equivariant map
\tau:X^{\ddagger}\to\pi_{0}(X^{\ddagger})\times\mathrm{Hom}(\mathbb{S}_{\mathbb{C}},P_{\Phi^{\ddagger},\mathbb{C}})
, where
D_{\Phi^{\ddagger}}
is a
P_{\Phi^{\ddagger}}(\mathbb{R})U_{\Phi^{\ddagger}}(\mathbb{C})
-orbit of
X^{{\ddagger},+}
. Since
P_{\Phi^{\ddagger}}(\mathbb{R})
acts transitively on
\pi_{0}(X^{\ddagger})\cong\{\pm 1\}
,
D_{\Phi^{\ddagger}}
does not depend on the choice of
X^{{\ddagger},+}
, and
\pi_{0}(D_{\Phi^{\ddagger}})=\pi_{0}(X^{\ddagger})
. Unless otherwise noted, we always let
D_{\Phi^{\ddagger}}^{+}:=\tau(X^{{\ddagger},+})
. We see in §
2.4
that points on
D^{+}_{\Phi^{\ddagger}}
correspond to mixed Hodge structures of
V
such that
\psi
is a (
positive
) polarization.

Let
\nu_{G}:(G_{0},X_{0})\to(T^{\prime},Y)
be the morphism between pure Shimura data induced by the quotient of
(G_{0},X_{0})
by
G^{\mathrm{der}}_{0}
, where
T^{\prime}:=G_{0}/G^{\mathrm{der}}_{0}
. Note that there is a commutative diagram

(1.3)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(G_{0},X_{0})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(G^{\ddagger},X^{\ddagger})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(T^{\prime},Y)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathbb{G}_{m},\mathbb{H}_{0}^{\pm}),}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nu_{G}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nu}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nu_{G}^{\prime}}

where
\nu_{G}^{\prime}
is defined by the quotient of
(T^{\prime},Y)
by
\nu_{G}(G_{0}\cap G^{{\ddagger},\mathrm{der}})
.

Lemma 1.6
.

The map
\nu^{\prime}_{G}:Y\to\mathbb{H}_{0}^{\pm}
is injective. The stabilizer
T^{\prime}(\mathbb{R})_{+}
of any point of
Y
in
T^{\prime}(\mathbb{R})
is connected.

Proof.

Let
\omega:\mathbb{G}_{m}\to G_{0}
be the weight cocharacter. Then
\nu_{G}^{\prime}\circ\nu_{G}\circ\omega
is isomorphic to
\mathbb{G}_{m}\xrightarrow{x\mapsto x^{2}}\mathbb{G}_{m}
.
Denote by
C\cong\mu_{2}
the kernel of this composition.

Let
G_{0}^{\prime}:=G^{{\ddagger},\mathrm{der}}\cap G_{0}
. There is a commutative diagram of algebraic groups

(1.4)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{G}_{m}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G_{0}^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{G}_{m},}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nu\circ\omega}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\omega}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nu_{G}^{\prime}\circ\nu_{G}}

where the horizontal row is exact.
Hence, there is an almost-direct product decomposition
G_{0}\cong G_{0}^{\prime}\times\omega(\mathbb{G}_{m})/C
for
G_{0}
. In what follows, we split the proof into two cases, according to whether or not the two-point finite group
C
is contained in
G_{0}^{\mathrm{der}}
.

If
C
lies in
G^{\mathrm{der}}_{0}
, then
T^{\prime}\cong\mathbb{K}\times\mathbb{G}_{m}
, where
\mathbb{K}
is the kernel of
\nu_{G}^{\prime}
. In fact,
T^{\prime}\cong\mathbb{K}\times(\nu_{G}\circ\omega(\mathbb{G}_{m}))/C^{\prime}
, where
C^{\prime}
is the image of
C
under
\nu_{G}
, which is trivial in this case. So
\nu_{G}^{\prime}
is the projection of
T^{\prime}\cong\mathbb{K}\times\mathbb{G}_{m}
to the second factor. Note that
\mathbb{K}
is a
connected and
\mathbb{R}
-anisotropic
torus since
T^{\prime}
is connected and
h_{x}(i)
induces a Cartan involution of
(G^{\ddagger}/\mathbb{G}_{m})_{\mathbb{R}}
, so
\mathbb{K}(\mathbb{R})
is also a
connected compact
Lie group by a theorem of Chevalley
(see, e.g.,
[
66
, Sec. 3.1, Thm. 3.1, and Sec. 3.2, Cor. 1]
). Hence,
\nu^{\prime}_{G}:Y\to\mathbb{H}_{0}^{\pm}
is isomorphic to the morphism
\pi_{0}(T^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}))\to\pi_{0}(\mathbb{G}_{m}(\mathbb{R}))
induced by the natural projection
T^{\prime}_{\mathbb{R}}\to\mathbb{G}_{m,\mathbb{R}}
, and therefore is injective since
\mathbb{K}(\mathbb{R})
is connected. The second statement follows from the injectivity proved above, and the connectedness of
\mathbb{K}(\mathbb{R})
.

If
C
does not lie in
G^{\mathrm{der}}_{0}
, it follows that
C^{\prime}\cong\mu_{2}
. Let
U^{1}
be the kernel of
N
. Choose any
x\in X_{0}
. Then
U^{\prime}
, the image of
U^{1}
under
U^{1}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{S}\xrightarrow{\nu_{G}\circ h_{x}}T^{\prime}_{\mathbb{R}}
, is not trivial since
C^{\prime}
lies in it; so
U^{\prime}
is isomorphic to
U^{1}
. Since
T^{\prime}
is connected, it follows that
\mathbb{K}
has at most
2
connected components. But we claim
\mathbb{K}
is connected. In fact,
\mathbb{K}/C^{\prime}
is connected and
C^{\prime}
is contained in a compact connected subgroup
U^{1}
in the compact algebraic group
\mathbb{K}
, so
C^{\prime}
lies in the identity component of
\mathbb{K}
, and then
\mathbb{K}
is connected. Hence, both
\mathbb{K}/C^{\prime}(\mathbb{R})
and
\mathbb{K}(\mathbb{R})
are connected by Chevalley’s theorem (see, e.g.,
[
66
, Sec. 3.1, Thm. 3.1, and Sec. 3.2, Cor. 1]
).

Furthermore,
T^{\prime}(\mathbb{R})
is connected. To see this, we note that there is an extension of algebraic groups

0\to\mathbb{G}_{m}\to T^{\prime}\to\mathbb{K}/C^{\prime}\to 0.

Since
\mathbb{K}/C^{\prime}(\mathbb{R})
is connected and
T^{\prime}\to\mathbb{K}/C^{\prime}
is surjective, the induced homomorphism between
\mathbb{R}
-points
T^{\prime}(\mathbb{R})\to(\mathbb{K}/C^{\prime})(\mathbb{R})
is surjective. Then we find a fibration
\mathbb{G}_{m}(\mathbb{R})\to T^{\prime}(\mathbb{R})\to(\mathbb{K}/C^{\prime})(\mathbb{R})
.
We can consider the long exact sequence of
\pi_{0}
- and
\pi_{1}
- groups

\cdots\to\pi_{1}((\mathbb{K}/C^{\prime})(\mathbb{R}))\xrightarrow{\ \partial\ }\pi_{0}(\mathbb{G}_{m}(\mathbb{R}))\to\pi_{0}(T^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}))\to\pi_{0}((\mathbb{K}/C^{\prime})(\mathbb{R}))\to 0.

Since
U^{\prime}(\mathbb{R})
is a loop in
\mathbb{K}(\mathbb{R})\subset T^{\prime}(\mathbb{R})
containing
C^{\prime}(\mathbb{R})\cong\mu_{2}(\mathbb{R})=\{\pm 1\}\subset\nu_{G}\circ\omega(\mathbb{G}_{m}(\mathbb{R}))\cong\mathbb{G}_{m}(\mathbb{R})
, where the last isomorphism is the natural isomorphism induced by the quotient of
\omega(\mathbb{G}_{m})
by
G_{0}^{\mathrm{der}}\cap\omega(\mathbb{G}_{m})=1
. Then there is a half-loop
U^{+}\cong[-1,1]
in
U^{\prime}(\mathbb{R})
connecting
1
and
-1
in
C^{\prime}(\mathbb{R})
whose image in
(\mathbb{K}/C^{\prime})(\mathbb{R})
is a loop, denoted by
\overline{U}^{+}
. Hence, the monodromy action of the homotopy class of
\overline{U}^{+}
interchanges the two elements of
\pi_{0}(\mathbb{G}_{m}(\mathbb{R}))
; that is,
\partial
is surjective. Then we find that
T^{\prime}(\mathbb{R})
is connected in the second case.

As a result,
Y
has only one point in this case, and therefore
\nu_{G}^{\prime}:Y\to\mathbb{H}_{0}^{\pm}
is injective. The second statement follows from the connectedness of
T^{\prime}(\mathbb{R})
.
∎

Lemma 1.7
.

Let
(G,X)
be a Shimura datum of abelian type. Let
G(\mathbb{R})_{+}
be the inverse image of
G^{\mathrm{ad}}(\mathbb{R})^{+}
under
G(\mathbb{R})\to G^{\mathrm{ad}}(\mathbb{R})
. Then
G(\mathbb{R})_{+}
is connected if
(G,X)
satisfies the following condition:

•

There is a Hodge-type Shimura datum
(G_{0},X_{0})
and a central isogeny
\pi:G_{0}\to G
such that the kernel of
\pi
is in
G^{\mathrm{der}}_{0}
and
\pi
induces a morphism between Shimura data
(G_{0},X_{0})\to(G,X)
.

Proof.

Suppose that
(G,X),

(G_{0},X_{0})
and
\pi
are as in the statement. Then they fit into a commutative diagram:

(1.5)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(G_{0},X_{0})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(G^{\ddagger},X^{\ddagger})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(G,X)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(T^{\prime},Y)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathbb{G}_{m},\mathbb{H}_{0}^{\pm}).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nu}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nu^{\prime}_{G}}

By Lemma
1.6
, it suffices to show that
G^{\mathrm{der}}(\mathbb{R})_{+}:=G(\mathbb{R})_{+}\cap G^{\mathrm{der}}(\mathbb{R})
is connected.
Fix any
x\in X
. Let
X^{+}
be the connected component of
X
containing
x
. Since
G^{\mathrm{der}}(\mathbb{R})^{+}
maps surjectively to
G^{\mathrm{ad}}(\mathbb{R})^{+}
and is contained in
G(\mathbb{R})^{+}\subset G(\mathbb{R})_{+}
,
G^{\mathrm{der}}(\mathbb{R})_{+}
acts on
X^{+}
transitively.

Therefore, it suffices to show that the centralizer
\mathrm{Cent}_{G^{\mathrm{der}}(\mathbb{R})}(h_{x})
is connected. This fact can be extracted from the proof of
[
64
, Prop. 2.11]
. Since
G^{\mathrm{der}}_{\mathbb{R}}
is connected, the algebraic group
\mathrm{Cent}_{G^{\mathrm{der}}_{\mathbb{R}}}(h_{x})
is connected. The Lie algebra of
\mathrm{Cent}_{G^{\mathrm{der}}_{\mathbb{R}}}(h_{x})
is fixed by the Cartan involution induced by
h_{x}(i)
, which implies that
\mathrm{Cent}_{G^{\mathrm{der}}_{\mathbb{R}}}(h_{x})
is compact. Then the desired assertion follows by Chevalley’s theorem (see, e.g.,
[
66
, Sec. 3.1, Thm. 3.1, and Sec. 3.2, Cor. 1]
).
∎

Proposition 1.8
.

Let
(G,\mathcal{X},\hbar)
be a pure Shimura datum with
\hbar:\mathcal{X}\to X=\mathrm{Hom}(\mathbb{S},G_{\mathbb{R}})
. Suppose that
(G,X)
satisfies the condition in Lemma
1.7
. Then for any neat open compact subgroup
K
, the pure Shimura variety
\mathrm{Sh}_{K}(G,\mathcal{X})
is a Shimura variety.

Proof.

By definition, it suffices to show that the
G(\mathbb{R})
-equivariant projection
\mathcal{X}\to\mathrm{Hom}(\mathbb{S},G_{\mathbb{R}})
is injective. Let
\mathcal{X}^{+}
be a connected component of
\mathcal{X}
, which maps to a connected component
X^{+}
of
X
. By
[
64
, Prop. 2.11 and Cor. 2.12]
,
\hbar
is a finite covering and
\mathcal{X}
is a union of hermitian symmetric domains with
G(\mathbb{R})^{+}
acting on each connected component transitively. Then the stabilizer of
\mathcal{X}^{+}
in
G(\mathbb{R})
is a subgroup of
G(\mathbb{R})_{+}
containing
G(\mathbb{R})^{+}
. By Lemma
1.7
,
G(\mathbb{R})^{+}=G(\mathbb{R})_{+}
, which implies the desired statement.
∎

Corollary 1.9
.

Let
(G,X)
be a Shimura datum satisfying the condition in Lemma
1.7
. Let
\Phi
be any cusp label representative. Then
(P_{\Phi,h},D_{\Phi,h})
defined in §
1.1.3
is a Shimura datum in the usual sense. If
(G,X)=(G_{0},X_{0})
is of Hodge type,
(P_{\Phi,h},D_{\Phi,h})
is of Hodge type and, in particular,
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi},h}
is a Hodge-type Shimura variety for any neat open compact subgroup
K\subset G(\mathbb{A}_{f})
.

Proof.

We first assume that
(G,X)
is a Hodge-type Shimura datum. Let
\iota:(G,X)\hookrightarrow(G^{\ddagger},X^{\ddagger})
be the embedding into the Siegel Shimura datum. Let
\Phi=(Q_{\Phi},[x_{\Phi}],g_{\Phi})
be a cusp label representative of
(G,X)
.
There is a unique minimal admissible
\mathbb{Q}
-parabolic subgroup
Q^{\ddagger}_{\Phi}
of
G^{\ddagger}
containing
Q_{\Phi}
. Let
[\iota(x_{\Phi})]
be the unique connected component of
X^{\ddagger}
containing
\iota(x_{\Phi})
. Then
(Q^{\ddagger}_{\Phi},[\iota(x_{\Phi})],\iota(g_{\Phi}))
is a cusp label representative of
(G^{\ddagger},X^{\ddagger})
, denoted by
\Phi^{\ddagger}
. Levi decomposition induces an injective homomorphism
P_{\Phi,h}\hookrightarrow P_{\Phi^{\ddagger},h}
. By
[
64
, 4.25]
, the group
P_{\Phi^{\ddagger},h}
is a symplectic similitude group and
\hbar(D_{\Phi^{\ddagger},h})
is the Siegel upper and lower half-spaces associated with
P_{\Phi^{\ddagger},h}
. By Proposition
1.8
,
(P_{\Phi,h},D_{\Phi,h})=\hbar(P_{\Phi,h},D_{\Phi,h})\hookrightarrow\hbar(P_{\Phi^{\ddagger},h},D_{\Phi^{\ddagger},h})=(P_{\Phi^{\ddagger},h},D_{\Phi^{\ddagger},h})
is a Hodge embedding since
\mathrm{Hom}(\mathbb{S},P_{\Phi,h,\mathbb{R}})\hookrightarrow\mathrm{Hom}(\mathbb{S},P_{\Phi^{\ddagger},h,\mathbb{R}})
.

Now we only assume that
(G,X)
satisfies the condition in Lemma
1.7
. Since
\pi:G_{0}\to G
is a central isogeny,
Q
is an admissible
\mathbb{Q}
-parabolic subgroup of
G
if and only if
\pi^{-1}(Q)
is an admissible
\mathbb{Q}
-parabolic subgroup of
G_{0}
. Since this question is not related to
g_{\Phi}
, we can choose a cusp label representative
\Phi=(Q_{\Phi},[x_{\Phi}],g_{\Phi})
of
(G,X)
such that there is a connected component
[x_{\Phi_{0}}]
of
X_{0}
mapping to
[x_{\Phi}]
and a lifting
g_{\Phi_{0}}
of
g_{\Phi}
to
G_{0}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
. Then
\Phi_{0}=(\pi^{-1}(Q),[x_{\Phi_{0}}],g_{\Phi_{0}})
is a cusp label representative of
(G_{0},X_{0})
. By the last paragraph and Proposition
1.8
,
({P}_{\Phi_{0},h},D_{\Phi_{0},h})
is a Hodge-type Shimura datum, and therefore
(P_{\Phi,h},D_{\Phi,h})
satisfies the condition in Lemma
1.7
. Hence, the statement follows from Proposition
1.8
.
∎

Corollary 1.10
.

With the conventions above, if
(G,X)=(G_{0},X_{0})
is of Hodge type and if
\Phi
maps to
\Phi^{\ddagger}\in\mathcal{CLR}(G^{\ddagger},X^{\ddagger})
(see the conventions in Corollary
1.9
), the morphisms
(P_{\Phi},D_{\Phi})\to(P_{\Phi^{\ddagger}},D_{\Phi^{\ddagger}})
,
(\overline{P}_{\Phi},\overline{D}_{\Phi})\to(\overline{P}_{\Phi^{\ddagger}},\overline{D}_{\Phi^{\ddagger}})
and
(P_{\Phi,h},D_{\Phi,h})\to(P_{\Phi^{\ddagger},h},D_{\Phi^{\ddagger},h})
induced by the Hodge embedding
(G,X)\hookrightarrow(G^{\ddagger},X^{\ddagger})
are embeddings. The last morphism
(P_{\Phi,h},D_{\Phi,h})\hookrightarrow(P_{\Phi^{\ddagger},h},D_{\Phi^{\ddagger},h})
is also a Hodge embedding.

Proof.

From the proof above, we have that
(P_{\Phi,h},D_{\Phi,h})=\hbar(P_{\Phi,h},D_{\Phi,h})\hookrightarrow\hbar(P_{\Phi^{\ddagger},h},D_{\Phi^{\ddagger},h})=(P_{\Phi^{\ddagger},h},D_{\Phi^{\ddagger},h})
is a Hodge embedding. By
[
64
, Cor. 2.12]
, every connected component of
D_{\Phi}
(resp.
D_{\Phi^{\ddagger}}
) maps isomorphically to its image in
\hbar(D_{\Phi})
(resp.
\hbar(D_{\Phi^{\ddagger}})
). Since the fibers of projection
D_{\Phi}\to D_{\Phi,h}
(resp.
D_{\Phi^{\ddagger}}\to D_{\Phi^{\ddagger},h}
) are connected, this implies that
D_{\Phi}=\hbar(D_{\Phi})
(resp.
D_{\Phi^{\ddagger}}=\hbar(D_{\Phi^{\ddagger}})
). The same argument also applies to
\overline{D}_{\Phi}
and
\overline{D}_{\Phi^{\ddagger}}
.
∎

1.2.2.

We compare the group
P_{\Phi}
with the group
P^{\prime\prime}
in
[
26
, 2.7]
; from now on, denote the latter group by
P_{\Phi}^{\prime\prime}
.
More precisely, for any
\Phi
in
\mathcal{CLR}(G,X)
, let
P^{\prime\prime}_{\Phi}
be the maximal
\mathbb{Q}
-subgroup of
Q_{\Phi}
such that the homomorphism
P^{\prime\prime}_{\Phi}\to\mathrm{GL}(\lie U_{\Phi})
defined by the adjoint representation of
Q_{\Phi}
on
\mathrm{GL}(\lie U_{\Phi})
factors through
P^{\prime\prime}_{\Phi}\to\mathbb{G}_{m}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{GL}(\lie U_{\Phi})
, where the second homomorphism
\mathbb{G}_{m}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{GL}(\lie U_{\Phi})
is given by the
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-action on
\lie U_{\Phi}
by homotheties. The group
P^{\prime\prime}_{\Phi}
is a normal subgroup of
Q_{\Phi}
containing the center
Z_{G}
of
G
. By
[
64
, Prop. 2.14(a)]
,
P_{\Phi}\subset P^{\prime\prime}_{\Phi}
.
Following
[
26
]
, let
P_{\Phi}^{\prime}
be the kernel of the adjoint representation
Q_{\Phi}\to\mathrm{GL}(\lie U_{\Phi})
. Let
P_{\Phi}^{*}
be the smallest normal
\mathbb{Q}
-subgroup of
Q_{\Phi}
such that
u_{x}^{Q_{\Phi}}\circ h_{\infty}(i)
factors through it and the unipotent radical of
P_{\Phi}^{*}
is
W_{\Phi}
.

Lemma 1.11
.

Assume that
(G,X)
is a Shimura datum such that there is a Hodge-type Shimura datum
(G_{0},X_{0})
and a morphism
\pi:(G_{0},X_{0})\to(G,X)
such that the kernel of
\pi:G_{0}\to G
is finite and is in
G_{0}^{\mathrm{der}}
. Then the quotient group
P^{\prime\prime}_{\Phi}/Z_{G}\cdot P_{\Phi}
is of compact type.

Proof.

In fact, we will imitate some arguments in
[
64
, Lem. 4.9]
.
Let
x
be any point on
X
.
As the boundary weight cocharacter
\omega^{Q_{\Phi}}_{x,\infty}
is defined over
\mathbb{Q}
by assumption, we have
P^{\prime\prime}_{\Phi}=\omega^{Q_{\Phi}}_{x,\infty}(\mathbb{G}_{m})\cdot P^{\prime}_{\Phi}
and
P_{\Phi}\supset\omega^{Q_{\Phi}}_{x,\infty}(\mathbb{G}_{m})\cdot P_{\Phi}^{*}
. Note that, from the construction of
h_{\infty}
and
u_{x}^{Q_{\Phi}}
(see
[
64
, Prop. 4.6]
),
P_{\Phi}^{*}\subset P^{\prime}_{\Phi}
since the adjoint representation of
u_{x}^{Q_{\Phi}}\circ h_{\infty}(i)
on
\lie U_{\Phi}
is trivial. This implies that
\omega^{Q_{\Phi}}_{x,\infty}(\mathbb{G}_{m})\cdot P_{\Phi}^{*}
is an almost direct product.

Therefore, we are reduced to showing that
P^{\prime}_{\Phi}/Z_{G}\cdot P^{*}_{\Phi}
is of compact type, and it suffices to show that
h_{x,\infty}(i):=u_{x}^{Q_{\Phi}}\circ h_{\infty}(i)
induces a Cartan involution on
(P^{\prime}_{\Phi}/Z_{G}\cdot W_{\Phi})_{\mathbb{R}}
since
h_{x,\infty}(i)
factors through
P_{\Phi,\mathbb{C}}^{*}
.
Let
\widetilde{G}
be the centralizer of
\omega^{Q_{\Phi}}_{x,\infty}
in
Q_{\Phi}
, which is a connected reductive subgroup that is naturally isomorphic to the Levi quotient
Q_{\Phi}/W_{\Phi}
. The quotient
P^{\prime}_{\Phi}/Z_{G}\cdot W_{\Phi}
is a normal subgroup of
Q_{\Phi}/Z_{G}\cdot W_{\Phi}
.
By the definition of
\widetilde{G}
,
h_{x,\infty}(i)
factors through
\widetilde{G}_{\mathbb{C}}
.

Finally, we claim that
h_{x}(i)
and
h_{x,\infty}(i)
induce the same Cartan involution on
(\widetilde{G}/Z_{G})_{\mathbb{R}}
: To see this, note that the difference
h_{x}(i)\cdot h_{x,\infty}(i)^{-1}
lies in the image of
u_{x}^{Q_{\Phi}}(H_{0,\mathbb{C}}^{\mathrm{der}})
.
Moreover, note that the adjoint representation of
u_{x}^{Q_{\Phi}}(H_{0,\mathbb{C}}^{\mathrm{der}})
on
G
induces a trivial restriction on
\widetilde{G}
, since the kernel of this adjoint representation is a normal subgroup of a semisimple group, since the conjugation of
\omega_{x,\infty}^{Q_{\Phi}}\cdot\omega_{x,0}^{Q_{\Phi},-1}
on
\widetilde{G}
is trivial, and since the image of
u_{x}^{Q_{\Phi}}(U_{0})
is in
W_{\Phi,\mathbb{R}}
. Hence,
h_{x,\infty}(i)
induces a Cartan involution on
(Q_{\Phi}/Z_{G}\cdot W_{\Phi})_{\mathbb{R}}
and on
(P^{\prime}_{\Phi}/Z_{G}\cdot W_{\Phi})_{\mathbb{R}}
.
∎

1.3.
