Compactifications and connected components

Let
K\subset G(\mathbb{A}_{f})
be a neat open compact subgroup. Let
X^{+}
be a connected component of
X
. Define
G(\mathbb{Q})_{+}:=G(\mathbb{Q})\cap G(\mathbb{R})_{+}
. We have

\mathrm{Sh}_{K}(G,X)(\mathbb{C})=G(\mathbb{Q})_{+}\backslash X^{+}\times G(\mathbb{A}_{f})/K\cong\coprod_{g_{i}\in I}X^{+}/\Gamma(g_{i}).

Here,
\Gamma(g_{i}):=G(\mathbb{Q})_{+}\cap g_{i}Kg_{i}^{-1}
and
I
is a set of representatives of the double coset
\pi_{0}(\mathrm{Sh}_{K}(G,X)_{\mathbb{C}})\cong G(\mathbb{Q})_{+}\backslash G(\mathbb{A}_{f})/K
. This disjoint union admits an algebraic structure
\mathrm{Sh}_{K}(G,X)_{\mathbb{C}}\cong\coprod_{g_{i}\in I}X^{+}/\Gamma(g_{i})^{\mathrm{alg}}
over
\mathbb{C}
.

In this subsection, we recall some key definitions and statements in Pink’s thesis on minimal and toroidal compactifications of
\mathrm{Sh}_{K}:=\mathrm{Sh}_{K}(G,X)
. Along the way, we also study the geometrically connected components of the strata of the toroidal and minimal compactifications of a single connected component
X^{+}/\Gamma(g_{i})^{\mathrm{alg}}
in the strata of the corresponding compactifications of
\mathrm{Sh}_{K}(G,X)_{\mathbb{C}}
.

1.3.1.

We summarize a situation that will repeatedly occur.

Let
(X,X_{1})
be a pair of topological spaces with an open and closed embedding
h:X\hookrightarrow X_{1}
. Let
(G,G_{1})
be a pair of groups equipped with an injective homomorphism
i:G\hookrightarrow G_{1}
. Let
K
(resp.
K_{1}
) be a subgroup of
G
(resp.
G_{1}
). Let
H
(resp.
H_{1}
) be a subgroup of
G
(resp.
G_{1}
). Suppose that
H
(resp.
H_{1}
) acts on
X
(resp.
X_{1}
) by
c:H\times X\to X
(resp.
c_{1}:H_{1}\times X_{1}\to X_{1}
), and
c
,
c_{1}
,
h
and
i
satisfy the following commutative diagram

(1.6)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H\times X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H_{1}\times X_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{1},}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(i,h)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c_{1}}

In addition, we assume that
H_{1}X=X_{1}
, and that
H_{1}
stabilizes
G
by conjugation.

We have left group actions of
H_{1}
on
X_{1}
and
G_{1}
, and a right group action of
K_{1}
on
G_{1}
.
Then we have the following decomposition of double cosets

H_{1}\backslash X_{1}\times G_{1}/K_{1}=H_{1}\backslash\coprod_{h\in I_{1}}h\cdot X\times G_{1}/K_{1}\cong\mathrm{Stab}_{H_{1}}(X)\backslash X\times G_{1}/K_{1},

where
I_{1}
is a set of representatives of the coset
H_{1}/\mathrm{Stab}_{H_{1}}(X)
.
Moreover,

\mathrm{Stab}_{H_{1}}(X)\backslash X\times G_{1}/K_{1}=\mathrm{Stab}_{H_{1}}(X)\backslash X\times\coprod_{g\in I_{2}}\mathrm{Stab}_{H_{1}}(X)GgK_{1}/K_{1},

where
I_{2}
is a set of representatives of the double coset
\mathrm{Stab}_{H_{1}}(X)G\backslash G_{1}/K_{1}.
If
i(K)\subset gK_{1}g^{-1}
, we also note that there is a canonical map from
H\backslash X\times G/K
to
\mathrm{Stab}_{H_{1}}(X)\backslash X\times\mathrm{Stab}_{H_{1}}(X)GgK_{1}/K_{1}.

Since
H_{1}
stabilizes
G
by assumption, we can further write

\mathrm{Stab}_{H_{1}}(X)\backslash X\times\mathrm{Stab}_{H_{1}}(X)GgK_{1}/K_{1}\cong\mathrm{Stab}_{H_{1}}(X)\cap GgK_{1}g^{-1}\backslash X\times GgK_{1}/K_{1}.

1.3.2.

First, we consider the minimal compactification.
For any cusp label representative
(Q_{\Phi},X^{+}_{\Phi},g_{\Phi})
, from §
1.1.3
, we associate a
P_{\Phi}(\mathbb{R})U_{\Phi}(\mathbb{C})
-homogeneous space
D_{\Phi}
with
\Phi
, and
D_{\Phi,h}
is called a
rational boundary component
of
X
(see
[
6
, 3.5]
). Let
X^{*}=\bigcup D_{\Phi,h}
be the union of
D_{\Phi,h}
running over all rational boundary components of
X
(also including
X
itself), endowed with the so-called
Satake topology
(see
[
6
, 4.8]
and
[
64
, 6.2]
). Note that the assignment
\Phi\mapsto D_{\Phi,h}
is not injective: for any two cusp label representatives
\Phi=(Q_{\Phi},X^{+}_{\Phi},g_{\Phi})
and
\Phi^{\prime}=(Q_{\Phi^{\prime}},X^{+}_{\Phi^{\prime}},g_{\Phi^{\prime}})
, the associated rational boundary components
D_{\Phi,h}=D_{\Phi^{\prime},h}
if and only if

D_{\Phi}=D_{\Phi^{\prime}}
if and only if

Q_{\Phi}=Q_{\Phi^{\prime}}
and
P_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})X^{+}_{\Phi}=P_{\Phi^{\prime}}(\mathbb{Q})X^{+}_{\Phi^{\prime}}
.

The
minimal compactification
of
\mathrm{Sh}_{K}(G,X)(\mathbb{C})
is defined as
\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{min}}_{K}(G,X)(\mathbb{C}):=G(\mathbb{Q})\backslash X^{*}\times G(\mathbb{A}_{f})/K
, and it possesses a canonical structure of a normal variety
\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{min}}_{K}(G,X)
over the reflex field
E:=E(G,X)
. See
[
64
, 12.3]
.

Consider the
G(\mathbb{Q})
-orbit
\Psi=G(\mathbb{Q})\Phi
of
\Phi
and let
D_{\Psi}
be the union of
D_{g\Phi}
for
g\in G(\mathbb{Q})
. Similarly, let
D_{\Psi,h}
be the union of subspaces
D_{g\Phi,h}
of
X^{*}
for
g\in G(\mathbb{Q})
.
Denote by
\mathrm{Z}_{\Psi,K}(\mathbb{C}):=G(\mathbb{Q})\backslash D_{\Psi,h}\times G(\mathbb{A}_{f})/K
the locally closed subspace of
\mathrm{Sh}_{K}^{\mathrm{min}}(G,X)(\mathbb{C})
induced by the inclusion
D_{\Psi,h}\hookrightarrow X^{*}
.
By
[
64
, 6.3]
and Proposition
1.4
, we have
\mathrm{Z}_{\Psi,K}(\mathbb{C})=\mathrm{Stab}_{Q(\mathbb{Q})}(D_{\Phi,h})\backslash D_{\Phi,h}\times G(\mathbb{A}_{f})/K=\coprod_{[\Phi_{i}]\in I(Q_{\Phi})}\mathrm{Z}_{[\Phi_{i}],K}(\mathbb{C})
, where each

??=\Delta_{\Phi_{i},K}\backslash\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi_{i},h}}(G_{\Phi_{i},h},D_{\Phi_{i},h})(\mathbb{C}),

and

??:=\mathrm{Stab}_{Q_{\Phi_{i}}(\mathbb{Q})}(D_{\Phi_{i}})\cap P_{\Phi_{i}}(\mathbb{A}_{f})g_{\Phi_{i}}Kg_{\Phi_{i}}^{-1}/P_{\Phi_{i}}(\mathbb{Q}).

Indeed, by Proposition
1.4
, there is an isomorphism
\mathrm{Stab}_{Q(\mathbb{Q})}(D_{\Phi})P_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f})\backslash G(\mathbb{A}_{f})/K\cong I(Q_{\Phi})
mapping
[g_{i}]
in the double coset to
[\Phi_{i}]=[(Q_{\Phi},X^{+}_{\Phi},g_{i})]
, and
\Psi
only depends on the
G(\mathbb{Q})
-conjugacy class
[Q_{\Phi}]
of
Q_{\Phi}
. Since the strong approximation theorem holds for unipotent groups, we have
\mathrm{Z}_{[\Phi_{i}],K}(\mathbb{C})=\Delta_{\Phi_{i},K}\backslash(P_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})\backslash D_{\Phi,h}\times P_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f})/K_{\Phi_{i}})
. By §
1.3.1
, we can write the disjoint union as above. If we fix a set of representatives for
I(Q_{\Phi})
as above such that
\Phi_{i}=(Q_{\Phi},X_{\Phi}^{+},g_{i})
, then

\Delta_{\Phi_{i},K}\cong\mathrm{Stab}_{Q_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})}(D_{\Phi})\cap P_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f})g_{i}Kg_{i}^{-1}/P_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q}).

In other words, for any admissible
\mathbb{Q}
-parabolic subgroup
Q
, we can choose any
\Phi=(Q,X^{+}_{\Phi},g_{\Phi})
and define
\Psi
as above. Then
\mathrm{Z}_{\Psi,K}(\mathbb{C})
is the union of all
\mathrm{Z}_{[\Phi_{i}],K}(\mathbb{C})
associated with equivalence classes of cusp label representatives formed by the
G(\mathbb{Q})
-conjugacy class
[Q]
and other data. Hence, it makes sense to denote
\mathrm{Z}_{\Psi,K}(\mathbb{C})
as
\mathrm{Z}_{[Q],K}(\mathbb{C})
.

Then

(1.7)

\mathrm{Sh}_{K}^{\mathrm{min}}(G,X)(\mathbb{C})\cong\coprod_{[Q]}\mathrm{Z}_{[Q],K}(\mathbb{C}),

where the disjoint union runs over the conjugacy classes of admissible
\mathbb{Q}
-parabolic subgroups of
G
.

Let
\Phi=(Q,X^{+},g_{\Phi})
. Let
D^{+}_{\Phi}
be the connected component of
D_{\Phi}
containing
X^{+}
. Then the image
D^{+}_{\Phi,h}
of
D_{\Phi}^{+}
in
D_{\Phi,h}
is a connected component of
D_{\Phi,h}
. We have
\mathrm{Z}_{[\Phi_{i}],K}(\mathbb{C})\cong\mathrm{Stab}_{Q(\mathbb{Q})}(D_{\Phi})\backslash D_{\Phi,h}\times\mathrm{Stab}_{Q(\mathbb{Q})}(D_{\Phi})P_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f})/K_{\Phi_{i}}\cong\coprod_{c_{j}\in C(\Phi_{i})}D_{\Phi,h}^{+}/\Gamma_{\Phi_{i}}(c_{j})
, where
C(\Phi_{i})
is a set of representatives of the double coset
\pi_{0}(\mathrm{Z}_{[\Phi_{i}],K}(\mathbb{C}))\cong\mathrm{Stab}_{Q(\mathbb{Q})}(D^{+}_{\Phi})\backslash\mathrm{Stab}_{Q(\mathbb{Q})}(D_{\Phi})P_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f})/K_{\Phi_{i}}
and
\Gamma_{\Phi_{i}}(c_{j}):=\mathrm{Stab}_{Q(\mathbb{Q})}(D_{\Phi}^{+})\cap c_{j}K_{\Phi_{i}}c_{j}^{-1}.

By
[
64
, Prop. 9.24 and Thm. 11.8]
again,
\mathrm{Z}_{[\Phi],K}(\mathbb{C}),

\mathrm{Z}_{\Psi,K}(\mathbb{C})
and
\mathrm{Z}_{[Q],K}(\mathbb{C})
admit algebraic structures over the reflex field
E
of
(G,X)
, and we denote them by
??
,
\mathrm{Z}_{\Psi,K}
and
\mathrm{Z}_{[Q],K}
, respectively. We also adopt similar notation for their base changes (cf. Convention
1.2
).

We have the following observation from the discussions above:

Lemma 1.12
.

Denote by
\overline{E}
the algebraic closure of
E:=E(G,X)
.
Fix a connected component
X^{+}
of
X
so that we can define an isomorphism
\pi_{0}(\mathrm{Sh}_{K}(G,X)_{\overline{E}})\cong G(\mathbb{Q})_{+}\backslash G(\mathbb{A}_{f})/K
mapping
G(\mathbb{Q})_{+}\backslash X^{+}\times G(\mathbb{Q})_{+}K/K
to
[1]
.
Denote the connected component of
\mathrm{Sh}_{K}^{\mathrm{min}}(G,X)_{\overline{E}}
corresponding to
[g]
by
\mathrm{Sh}_{K,\overline{E}}^{\mathrm{min},[g]}
for
[g]\in G(\mathbb{Q})_{+}\backslash G(\mathbb{A}_{f})/K
.
Let
\mathrm{Z}_{[Q],K,\overline{E}}^{[g]}
be the pullback of
\mathrm{Z}_{[Q],K,\overline{E}}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{Sh}_{K}^{\mathrm{min}}(G,X)_{\overline{E}}
to
\mathrm{Sh}_{K,\overline{E}}^{\mathrm{min},[g]}
.
For any
Q
, there is an isomorphism
\pi_{0}(\mathrm{Z}_{[Q],K,\overline{E}})\cong\mathrm{Stab}_{Q(\mathbb{Q})}(D^{+}_{Q,X^{+}})\backslash G(\mathbb{A}_{f})/K
such that
\mathrm{Z}_{[Q],K,\overline{E}}^{[g]}\cong\coprod_{[g^{\prime}]\mapsto[g]}\mathrm{Z}_{[Q],K,\overline{E}}^{[g^{\prime}]}
, where the disjoint union runs over the preimages of
[g]
under the natural quotient

\pi_{Q,X^{+}}:\mathrm{Stab}_{Q(\mathbb{Q})}(D_{Q,X^{+}}^{+})\backslash G(\mathbb{A}_{f})/K\longrightarrow G(\mathbb{Q})_{+}\backslash G(\mathbb{A}_{f})/K.

Additionally, in the quotient above, the contribution of
\pi_{0}(\mathrm{Z}_{[\Phi],K,\overline{E}})
is a subquotient

\pi_{\Phi}:\mathrm{Stab}_{Q(\mathbb{Q})}(D^{+}_{Q,X^{+}})\cap P_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f})g_{\Phi}Kg^{-1}_{\Phi}\backslash P_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f})g_{\Phi}K/K\longrightarrow G(\mathbb{Q})_{+}\backslash G(\mathbb{A}_{f})/K.

Proof.

It suffices to check the statement over
\mathbb{C}
.
By the definition of Satake topology, the closure
X^{+,*}
of
X^{+}
in
X^{*}
has a stratification consisting of connected components
D^{+}_{\Phi,h}
of
D_{\Phi,h}
such that
D^{+}_{\Phi}
contains
\tau(X^{+})
. Hence,
\mathrm{Sh}_{K}^{\mathrm{min}}(G,X)(\mathbb{C})=G(\mathbb{Q})_{+}\backslash X^{+,*}\times G(\mathbb{A}_{f})/K
, and the strata in (
1.7
) can be written as
G(\mathbb{Q})_{+}\backslash G(\mathbb{Q})_{+}D^{+}_{\Phi,h}\times G(\mathbb{A}_{f})/K=\mathrm{Z}_{[Q],K}(\mathbb{C})
, for some connected component
D^{+}_{\Phi}
containing
\tau(X^{+})
. Then the map between the
\pi_{0}
of
\mathrm{Z}_{[Q],K,\mathbb{C}}
and
\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{min}}_{K}(G,X)(\mathbb{C})
can be written as
\pi_{0}(\mathrm{Z}_{[Q],K,\mathbb{C}})\cong G(\mathbb{Q})_{+}\backslash(G(\mathbb{Q})_{+}/\mathrm{Stab}_{Q(\mathbb{Q})}(D^{+}_{Q,X^{+}}))\times G(\mathbb{A}_{f})/K\to G(\mathbb{Q})_{+}\backslash(G(\mathbb{Q})_{+}\backslash G(\mathbb{Q})_{+})\times G(\mathbb{A}_{f})/K\cong\pi_{0}(\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{min}}_{K}(G,X)_{\mathbb{C}}).
We get the desired statement.
∎

For any cusp label
[\Phi]
with a representative
\Phi
and any geometrically connected component
\mathrm{Sh}^{+}_{K}
of
\mathrm{Sh}_{K}(G,X)
defined over
\overline{E}
, we denote by
\mathrm{Sh}_{K}^{+,\mathrm{min}}
the schematic closure of
\mathrm{Sh}_{K}^{+}
in
\mathrm{Sh}_{K,\overline{E}}^{\mathrm{min}}
and denote by
\mathrm{Z}_{[\Phi],K}^{+}
the pullback
\mathrm{Z}_{[\Phi],K,\overline{E}}\times_{\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{min}}_{K,\overline{E}}}\mathrm{Sh}^{+,\mathrm{min}}_{K}
.

Remark 1.13
.

Although in the theory of compactifications of connected Shimura varieties over
\mathbb{C}
(see
[
5
, Ch. III]
), the strata defined for minimal compactifications are all connected, the strata
\mathrm{Z}_{[\Phi],K}^{+}
induced from Pink’s theory for every connected component
\mathrm{Sh}_{K}^{+,\mathrm{min}}
are not connected in general.

1.3.3.

Now we recall Pink’s work on toroidal compactifications of
\mathrm{Sh}_{K}(G,X)
. Let
Z:=Z_{G}
be the center of
G
. Let
\Phi
be any cusp label representative. Denote by
Z_{\Phi}
the center of
P_{\Phi}
. Let
Z_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})^{\circ}
be the subgroup of
Z_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})
that acts trivially on
D_{\Phi}
. Let
K_{\Phi,W}
be the projection of
(Z_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})^{\circ}\times W_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f}))\cap K_{\Phi}
to the second factor
W_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
, and let
K_{\Phi,U}:=K_{\Phi,W}\cap U(\mathbb{A}_{f})
. So
K_{\Phi,U}
is the projection of
(Z_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})^{\circ}\times U_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f}))\cap K_{\Phi}
to the second factor.

Moreover, define
K^{\prime}_{\Phi,U}
(resp.
K^{\prime}_{\Phi,W}
) to be the projection of
(Z(\mathbb{Q})\times U_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f}))\cap g_{\Phi}Kg_{\Phi}^{-1}
(resp.
(Z(\mathbb{Q})\times W_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f}))\cap g_{\Phi}Kg_{\Phi}^{-1}
) to the second factor
U_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
.

Lemma 1.14
.

If
K
is neat,
K_{\Phi,U}
(resp.
K_{\Phi,W}
) is contained in
K_{\Phi,U}^{\prime}
(resp.
K_{\Phi,W}^{\prime}
) as a finite index subgroup. Moreover, if we assume that
Z
is isogenous to a product of
\mathbb{Q}
-split tori and
\mathbb{R}
-anisotropic tori, then
K_{\Phi,U}=K_{\Phi,U}^{\prime}
and
K_{\Phi,W}=K_{\Phi,W}^{\prime}
.

Proof.

We only prove the result for
K_{\Phi,U}
and
K_{\Phi,U}^{\prime}
.
By the proof of
[
64
, Cor. 4.10]
, the center of
ZP_{\Phi}/ZW_{\Phi}
is isogenous to a product of
\mathbb{Q}
-split tori and compact-type tori. Hence, if
K_{\Phi}
is neat, then
K_{\Phi,U}
is contained in the projection of
(Z(\mathbb{Q})\cdot Z_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})^{\circ}\times U_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f}))\cap g_{\Phi}Kg_{\Phi}^{-1}
to the second factor
U_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
, and the latter is isomorphic to
K_{\Phi,U}^{\prime}
. By neatness assumption of
K
, the only neat arithmetic subgroup of
Z(\mathbb{Q})
is the trivial group, so
K_{\Phi,U}=K^{\prime}_{\Phi,U}
.
∎

Define the lattice
??:=(U_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})\cap K_{\Phi,U})(-1)\subset U_{\Phi}(\mathbb{R})(-1)
, and let
??
be the algebraic torus whose cocharacter is the lattice
\mathbf{S}_{K_{\Phi}}^{\vee}
in
U_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})(-1)
. Let
??
be the union of all
\mathrm{int}(\gamma^{-1})\mathbf{P}_{\Phi^{\prime}}^{+}\subset U_{\Phi}(\mathbb{R})(-1)
such that
\Phi\xrightarrow[\sim]{(\gamma,q)_{K}}\Phi^{\prime}
. (Note that
\mathbf{P}_{\Phi}
is not a rational polyhedral cone.) Define
??:=(U_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})\cap K_{\Phi,U}^{\prime})(-1)\subset U_{\Phi}(\mathbb{R})(-1)
. By
[
64
, 3.13, Prop. 11.10 and Thm. 11.8]
,
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi}}
is a torus torsor under
\mathbf{E}_{K_{\Phi}}
over
\overline{\mathrm{Sh}}_{K_{\Phi}}
.

Let us recall the definitions related to cone decompositions.

Definition 1.15

(
[
64
, 6.4 and 7.12]
and
[
50
, 2.1.23]
)
.

An
admissible
rational polyhedral cone decomposition
\Sigma
for
(G,X,K)
is an association
\Phi\in\mathcal{CLR}(G,X)\mapsto\Sigma(\Phi)
, where
\Sigma(\Phi)
is a rational polyhedral cone decomposition of
\mathbf{P}_{\Phi}
, such that: if
\Phi_{1}\xrightarrow{(\gamma,p)_{K}}\Phi_{2}
,
\mathrm{int}(\gamma^{-1})\Sigma(\Phi_{2})=\Sigma(\Phi_{1})|_{\mathrm{int}(\gamma^{-1})\mathbf{P}_{\Phi_{2}}}
under the natural embedding
\mathrm{int}(\gamma^{-1}):U_{\Phi_{2}}(\mathbb{R})(-1)\hookrightarrow U_{\Phi_{1}}(\mathbb{R})(-1)
.

Let
\Sigma
be any admissible rational polyhedral cone decomposition. Then
\Sigma(\Phi)=\Sigma((q,k)\Phi)
for any
q\in P_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
and
k\in K
.
If
\Phi_{1}\xrightarrow[\sim]{(\gamma,q)_{K}}\Phi_{2}
, then
\gamma U_{\Phi_{1}}(\mathbb{R})\gamma^{-1}=U_{\Phi_{2}}(\mathbb{R})
. Note that any
\gamma\in G(\mathbb{Q})
has a left action on
\Sigma
such that for any cone
\sigma\in\Sigma(\Phi_{1})
where
\sigma^{\circ}\subset\mathbf{P}^{+}_{\Phi_{1}}
, the conjugation
\gamma\sigma\gamma^{-1}
induces a cone in
\Sigma(\Phi_{2})
whose interior lies in
\mathbf{P}^{+}_{\Phi_{2}}.

Hence,
G(\mathbb{Q})
acts on the set of pairs
(\Phi,\sigma)
where
\sigma\in\Sigma(\Phi)
such that
\sigma^{\circ}\subset\mathbf{P}^{+}_{\Phi}
. As in
[
50
, 2.1.26]
, we say any two such pairs
(\Phi_{1},\sigma_{1})
and
(\Phi_{2},\sigma_{2})
are
equivalent
if
\Phi_{1}\xrightarrow[\sim]{(\gamma,q)_{K}}\Phi_{2}
and
\gamma\sigma_{1}\gamma^{-1}=\sigma_{2}
; we denote this equivalence by
(\Phi_{1},\sigma_{1})\xrightarrow[\sim]{(\gamma,q)_{K}}(\Phi_{2},\sigma_{2})
. We denote the set of equivalence classes in the form
[(\Phi,\sigma)]
by ??, the elements in it are called
cusp labels with cones
. There is a partial order on
\mathrm{Cusp}_{K}(G,X,\Sigma)
defined as follows:
[(\Phi_{1},\sigma_{1})]\preceq[(\Phi_{2},\sigma_{2})]
if and only if
\Phi_{1}\xrightarrow{(\gamma,q)_{K}}\Phi_{2}
and
\gamma^{-1}\sigma_{2}\gamma
is a face of
\sigma_{1}
.

Define
\Sigma^{+}(\Phi)
to be the cones
\sigma\in\Sigma(\Phi)
satisfying
\sigma^{\circ}\subset\mathbf{P}^{+}_{\Phi}
.

From Proposition
1.4
, we can see that when we fix a
\Phi
, the stabilizer of
[\Phi]
in
Q_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})
is
\widetilde{\Delta}_{\Phi,K}:=\mathrm{Stab}_{Q_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})}(D_{\Phi})\cap P_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f})g_{\Phi}Kg^{-1}_{\Phi}
. Let
\Delta_{\Phi,K}:=\widetilde{\Delta}_{\Phi,K}/P_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})
. This means that for any
\Upsilon=[(\Phi,\sigma)]\in\mathrm{Cusp}_{K}(G,X,\Sigma)
,
\Upsilon
is determined by a pair
([\Phi],[\sigma])
, where
[\Phi]\in\mathrm{Cusp}_{K}(G,X)
and
[\sigma]
is a
\Delta_{\Phi,K}
-orbit of
\sigma
in
U_{\Phi}(\mathbb{R})
. Let
??
be the normal subgroup of
\Delta_{\Phi,K}
that stabilizes
\sigma
. By
[
50
, 2.1.19]
, when
K
is neat,
\Delta^{\circ}_{\Phi,K}
is independent of the choice of
\sigma
such that
\sigma^{\circ}\subset\mathbf{P}^{+}_{\Phi}
, and it fixes
\mathbf{P}_{\Phi}
.

Definition 1.16
.

Let
\Sigma
be any admissible rational polyhedral cone decomposition for
(G,X,K)
, we can further define the following terminologies:

•

The decomposition
\Sigma
is called
complete
if, for any
\Phi
,
\mathbf{P}_{\Phi}=\bigcup_{\sigma\in\Sigma(\Phi)}\sigma^{\circ}
.

•

The decomposition
\Sigma
is called
finite
if
G(\mathbb{Q})\backslash\Sigma/K
is finite.

•

The decomposition
\Sigma
is a decomposition
without self-intersections
if, for any
\tau,\sigma\in\Sigma(\Phi)
such that
\tau
is a face of
\sigma
and
\sigma^{\circ}\subset\mathbf{P}_{\Phi}^{+}
, and any
\gamma\in G(\mathbb{Q})
,
q\in P_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
and
k\in K
such that
(\gamma,q,k)\tau
is also a face of
\sigma
, we have that
(\gamma,q,k)\tau=\tau
. (This condition is equivalent to
[
44
, Condition 6.2.5.25]
if
K
is neat; see
[
44
, Rmk. 6.2.5.26]
and the discussion above.)

•

The decomposition
\Sigma
is
smooth
(resp.
projective
) if
\Sigma(\Phi)
, as a cone decomposition of
\mathbf{P}_{\Phi}\subset U_{\Phi}(\mathbb{R})(-1)
, is smooth (resp. projective) with respect to
\mathbf{\Lambda}_{K_{\Phi}}
for any
\Phi
; note that, if
(G,X)
is a Hodge-type Shimura datum, we can replace the
\mathbf{\Lambda}_{K_{\Phi}}
in the last sentence with
\mathbf{S}_{K_{\Phi}}^{\vee}
by Lemma
1.14
.

In the last paragraph, being “smooth with respect to
\mathbf{\Lambda}_{K_{\Phi}}
” has the following precise meaning: We require that, for any
\sigma\in\Sigma(\Phi)
, there is a
\mathbb{Z}
-basis
\{v_{1},v_{2},...,v_{n}\}
of
\mathbf{\Lambda}_{K_{\Phi}}
such that
\sigma=\sum_{i=1}^{k}\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}v_{i}
for some
k\leq n
.

Moreover, being “projective with respect to
\mathbf{\Lambda}_{K_{\Phi}}
” has the following precise meaning: We require that there is a polarization function
\mathrm{pol}_{K_{\Phi}}:\mathbf{P}_{\Phi}\to\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
on
\mathbf{P}_{\Phi}
such that

–

\mathrm{pol}_{K_{\Phi}}
is continuous and
\Delta_{\Phi,K}
-invariant, and is positive on
\mathbf{P}_{\Phi}\backslash\{0\}
,

–

the restriction
\mathrm{pol}_{K_{\Phi}}|_{\mathbf{\Lambda}_{K_{\Phi}}\cap\mathbf{P}_{\Phi}}
takes values in
\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
,

–

\mathrm{pol}_{K_{\Phi}}
is piecewise linear, and is linear on a rational polyhedral cone
\sigma\subset\mathbf{P}_{\Phi}
if and only if
\sigma\in\Sigma(\Phi)
, and

–

we have
\mathrm{pol}_{K_{\Phi}}(x+y)\geq\mathrm{pol}_{K_{\Phi}}(x)+\mathrm{pol}_{K_{\Phi}}(y)
for any
x
and
y
in
\mathbf{P}_{\Phi}
.

Convention 1.17
.

We always choose
\Sigma
to be admissible, complete, finite, and without self-intersections. From now on, whenever we say
\Sigma
is an admissible cone decomposition, it will mean that
\Sigma
is an admissible rational polyhedral cone decomposition that is complete, finite, and without self-intersections. The refinements of cone decompositions we take in this paper are also admissible, complete, finite, and without self-intersections.

Definition 1.18

(cf.
[
47
]
)
.

Let
f:(G_{1},X_{1})\to(G_{2},X_{2})
be a morphism of Shimura data. Let
g\in G_{2}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
. Let
K_{1}\subset G_{1}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
and
K_{2}\subset G_{2}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
be neat open compact subgroups such that
f(K_{1})\subset gK_{2}g^{-1}
. By
[
64
, 3.5 and Thm. 12.4]
, there is a morphism over the reflex field of
(G_{1},X_{1})
:

f(g)_{K_{1},K_{2}}:\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{1}}(G_{1},X_{1})\to\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{2}}(G_{2},X_{2}).

(1)

Suppose that
(G_{1},X_{1})=(G_{2},X_{2})=(G,X)
and
f=\mathrm{id}
. Then, in this case,
f(g)_{K_{1},K_{2}}=[g]_{K_{1},K_{2}}
is the right action of
g
. Suppose that there is an admissible cone decomposition
\Sigma
for
(G,X,K_{2})
, by
[
64
, 6.7]
, the pullback of
\Sigma
, denoted by
[g]_{K_{1},K_{2}}^{*}(\Sigma)
is an admissible cone decomposition for
(G,X,K_{1})
. We say
[g]_{K_{1},K_{2}}^{*}(\Sigma)
is
induced
by
\Sigma
under
[g]_{K_{1},K_{2}}
.

(2)

Suppose that there are admissible cone decompositions
\Sigma_{1}
for
(G_{1},X_{1},K_{1})
and
\Sigma_{2}
for
(G_{2},X_{2},K_{2})
. Suppose that for any
\Phi_{1}\in\mathcal{CLR}(G_{1},X_{1})
mapping to some
\Phi_{2}\in\mathcal{CLR}(G_{2},X_{2})
under
f(g)_{K_{1},K_{2}}
, the induced map
U_{\Phi_{1}}(\mathbb{R})(-1)\to U_{\Phi_{2}}(\mathbb{R})(-1)
is injective. Then we say that
\Sigma_{1}
is
induced
by
\Sigma_{2}
under
f(g)_{K_{1},K_{2}}
if, for any
\sigma_{1}\in\Sigma_{1}(\Phi_{1})
,
\sigma_{1}
is exactly the preimage of some
\sigma_{2}\in\Sigma_{2}(\Phi_{2})
. Denote
\Sigma_{1}:=f(g)_{K_{1},K_{2}}^{*}(\Sigma_{2})
.
If the image of
\sigma_{1}
is exactly
\sigma_{2}\in\Sigma_{2}(\Phi_{2})
itself for any
\sigma_{1}\in\Sigma_{1}(\Phi_{1})
and any pair
(\Phi_{1},\Phi_{2})
described above, then we say that
\Sigma_{1}
and
\Sigma_{2}
are
strictly compatible
with each other.

(3)

Suppose that there are admissible cone decompositions
\Sigma_{1}
for
(G_{1},X_{1},K_{1})
and
\Sigma_{2}
for
(G_{2},X_{2},K_{2})
. We say that
\Sigma_{1}
and
\Sigma_{2}
are
compatible
with each other if, for any
\Phi_{1}\in\mathcal{CLR}(G_{1},X_{1})
mapping to
\Phi_{2}\in\mathcal{CLR}(G_{2},X_{2})
under
f(g)_{K_{1},K_{2}}
, the image of any
\sigma_{1}\in\Sigma_{1}(\Phi_{1})
under the map
\mathbf{P}_{\Phi_{1}}\to\mathbf{P}_{\Phi_{2}}
is contained in some
\Sigma_{2}\in\Sigma_{2}(\Phi_{2})
.

Let
\Sigma
be an admissible cone decomposition for
(G,X,K)
. Assume that
K
is a neat open compact subgroup of
G(\mathbb{A}_{f})
. By the main theorems of
[
64
]
, there is a toroidal compactification
\mathrm{Sh}_{K}^{\Sigma}:=\mathrm{Sh}_{K}^{\Sigma}(G,X)
of
\mathrm{Sh}_{K}
over the reflex field
E:=E(G,X)
, such that
\mathrm{Sh}_{K}^{\Sigma}
is a normal algebraic space that is proper over
\spec E
. If
\Sigma
is smooth (resp. projective), then
\mathrm{Sh}_{K}^{\Sigma}
is smooth with a normal crossings boundary divisor
D:=(\mathrm{Sh}_{K}^{\Sigma}\backslash\mathrm{Sh}_{K})_{\mathrm{red}}
(resp. is projective and representable by a scheme). There is a good stratification labeled by
\mathrm{Cusp}_{K}(G,X,\Sigma)
:

\mathrm{Sh}_{K}^{\Sigma}\cong\coprod\limits_{\Upsilon\in\mathrm{Cusp}_{K}(G,X,\Sigma)}\mathrm{Z}_{\Upsilon,K}.

For any
\Upsilon=[(\Phi,\sigma)]\in\mathrm{Cusp}_{K}(G,X,\Sigma)
, the stratum
??
in
\mathrm{Sh}_{K}^{\Sigma}
is a normal variety over
E
and is isomorphic to
\Delta^{\circ}_{\Phi,K}\backslash\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi},\sigma}
, where
??
is the
\sigma
-stratum of the twisted affine torus embedding
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi}}\hookrightarrow??
over
\overline{\mathrm{Sh}}_{K_{\Phi}}
defined by
\Sigma^{+}(\Phi)
. Note that
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi},\sigma}
is closed in
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi}}(\sigma)
and is locally closed in
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi}}(\Sigma)
.

Moreover, there is a canonical isomorphism:

(\mathrm{Sh}_{K}^{\Sigma})^{\widehat{\ }}_{\mathrm{Z}_{\Upsilon,K}}\cong\Delta^{\circ}_{\Phi,K}\backslash\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi}}(\sigma)^{\widehat{\ }}_{\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi},\sigma}}.

Let
\widetilde{\Delta}^{\circ}_{\Phi,K}
be the preimage of
\Delta^{\circ}_{\Phi,K}
in
\mathrm{Stab}_{Q(\mathbb{Q})}(D_{\Phi})\cap P_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f})g_{\Phi}Kg_{\Phi}^{-1}
. Since
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi},\sigma}
is the closed stratum of the twisted affine torus embedding
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi}}\hookrightarrow??
over an abelian scheme torsor
\overline{\mathrm{Sh}}_{K_{\Phi}}
over
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi},h}
(see
[
64
, 3.21]
and
[
50
, 2.1.10]
),
\pi_{0}(\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi},\sigma,\mathbb{C}})\cong\pi_{0}(\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi},h,\mathbb{C}})\cong\mathrm{Stab}_{P_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})}(D_{\Phi}^{+})\backslash P(\mathbb{A}_{f})/K_{\Phi}.
Moreover,
\pi_{0}(\mathrm{Sh}_{K,\mathbb{C}}^{\Sigma})\cong\pi_{0}(\mathrm{Sh}_{K,\mathbb{C}})
since
\mathrm{Sh}_{K}
is dense in
\mathrm{Sh}_{K}^{\Sigma}
.

We have the following observation:

Lemma 1.19
.

Let
X^{+}
and
Q
be as in Lemma
1.12
.
For any
[g]\in G(\mathbb{Q})_{+}\backslash G(\mathbb{A}_{f})/K
and any
\Upsilon=[(\Phi,\sigma)]\in\mathrm{Cusp}_{K}(G,X,\Sigma)
with
\Phi=(Q,X^{+},g_{\Phi})
so that
[\Phi]\in I(Q)
, denote by
\mathrm{Z}_{\Upsilon,\overline{E}}^{[g]}
(resp.
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi},\sigma,\overline{E}}^{[g]}
) the pullback of
\mathrm{Z}_{\Upsilon,\overline{E}}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{Sh}_{K,\overline{E}}^{\Sigma}
(resp.
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi},\sigma,\overline{E}}\to\mathrm{Sh}^{\Sigma}_{K,\overline{E}}
) to the connected component
\mathrm{Sh}_{K,\overline{E}}^{\Sigma,[g]}
corresponding to
[g]
. Then
\mathrm{Z}_{\Upsilon,\overline{E}}^{[g]}=\coprod_{[g^{\prime}]\mapsto[g]}\mathrm{Z}_{\Upsilon,\overline{E}}^{[g^{\prime}]}
and
\mathrm{Sh}^{[g]}_{K_{\Phi},\sigma,\overline{E}}=\coprod_{[g^{\prime\prime}]\mapsto[g]}\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi},\sigma,\overline{E}}^{[g^{\prime\prime}]}
,
where the first disjoint union runs over the preimage of
[g]
under

(1.8)

\pi_{0}(\mathrm{Z}_{\Upsilon,\overline{E}})\cong\mathrm{Stab}_{\widetilde{\Delta}^{\circ}_{\Phi,K}}(D^{+}_{Q,X^{+}})\backslash P_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f})g_{\Phi}K/K\longrightarrow\pi_{0}(\mathrm{Sh}_{K,\overline{E}}),

and
where the second disjoint union runs over the preimage of
[g]
under

(1.9)

\pi_{0}(\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi},\sigma,\overline{E}})=\mathrm{Stab}_{P_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})}(D^{+}_{Q,X^{+}})\backslash P_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f})g_{\Phi}K/K\longrightarrow\pi_{0}(\mathrm{Sh}_{K,\overline{E}}).

Proof.

It suffices to check this over
\mathbb{C}
. Since
g_{\Phi}
is a representative of
\mathrm{Stab}_{Q(\mathbb{Q})}(D_{\Phi})P_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f})\backslash G(\mathbb{A}_{f})/K
, and

\mathrm{Stab}_{P_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})}(D_{\Phi}^{+})\backslash P_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f})/K_{\Phi}
is isomorphic to
\mathrm{Stab}_{P_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})}(D_{\Phi}^{+})\backslash P_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f})g_{\Phi}K/K
,
the map between connected components
\pi_{0}(\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi},\sigma,\mathbb{C}})\to\pi_{0}(\mathrm{Z}_{[Q],K,\mathbb{C}})
induced by the natural morphism
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi},\sigma,\mathbb{C}}\to\mathrm{Z}_{[Q],K,\mathbb{C}}
is the map
\mathrm{Stab}_{P_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})}(D_{\Phi}^{+})\backslash P_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f})g_{\Phi}K/K\to\mathrm{Stab}_{Q(\mathbb{Q})}(D_{\Phi}^{+})\backslash G(\mathbb{A}_{f})/K
induced by
P_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f})g_{\Phi}K\hookrightarrow G(\mathbb{A}_{f})
and
\mathrm{Stab}_{P_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})}(D_{\Phi}^{+})\hookrightarrow\mathrm{Stab}_{Q(\mathbb{Q})}(D_{\Phi}^{+})
. Then the statement follows from Lemma
1.12
and the paragraph above.
∎

Remark 1.20
.

Let
\mathrm{Sh}_{K}^{+}
be a geometrically connected component of
\mathrm{Sh}_{K}(G,X)
. Denote by
\mathrm{Sh}_{K}^{+,\Sigma}
and
\mathrm{Sh}_{K}^{+,\mathrm{min}}
its closure in
\mathrm{Sh}_{K}^{\Sigma}(G,X)
and
\mathrm{Sh}_{K}^{\mathrm{min}}(G,X)
respectively. The components
\mathrm{Sh}_{K}^{+,\Sigma}
and
\mathrm{Sh}_{K}^{+,\mathrm{min}}
might not intersect with all strata of the toroidal and minimal compactifications nontrivially (see the appendix of
[
74
]
for an affirmative answer about this for the PEL type A and C case); moreover, from (
1.8
), we can see that for toroidal compactifications, each stratum appearing in some
\mathrm{Sh}_{K}^{+,\Sigma}
, if not trivial, might not be geometrically connected, since
\Delta^{\circ}_{\Phi,K}
might be trivial and the action of
\Delta_{\Phi,K}
might not be trivial, and since
\mathrm{Z}_{[\Phi],K}^{+}
might not be connected.

Convention 1.21
.

If
K
is clear in the context, we will omit the subscript
K
in
\Delta_{\Phi,K}
,
\Delta_{\Phi,K}^{\circ}
,
\mathrm{Z}_{[\Phi],K}
,
\mathrm{Z}_{[Q],K}
and
\mathrm{Z}_{[\Phi],K}^{+}
.

1.3.4.

We have the following generalization of
[
36
, Lem. 2.1.2]
for mixed Shimura varieties.

Lemma 1.22
.

Let
f:(P^{1},\mathcal{X}_{1})\hookrightarrow(P^{2},\mathcal{X}_{2})
be an embedding of two mixed Shimura data. For
i=1
or
2
, assume that
K^{i}:=K_{p}^{i}K^{i,p}
, where
K_{p}^{i}
is an open compact subgroup of
P^{i}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
and
K^{i,p}\subset P^{i}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
is neat open compact. Assume that
K^{2}_{p}\cap P^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})=K^{1}_{p}
. Then, for any
K^{1,p}
, there is a
K^{2,p}
containing
K^{1,p}
such that
f
induces a closed embedding

(1.10)

\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{1}}(P^{1},X_{1})\longrightarrow\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{2}}(P^{2},X_{2}).

Proof.

This is essentially
[
36
, Lem. 2.1.2]
, with some inputs in
[
17
]
replaced by those in
[
64
]
.
In fact, since (
1.10
) is finite, it suffices to show that

\varprojlim_{K^{1,p}}\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{1,p}K_{p}^{1}}(P^{1},\mathcal{X}_{1})\longrightarrow\varprojlim_{K^{2,p}}\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{2,p}K_{p}^{2}}(P^{2},\mathcal{X}_{2})

is injective, where the inverse limits run over all neat open compact
K^{i,p}
for
i=1,2
.
Let
Z^{i}(\mathbb{Q})^{\circ}
be the subgroup of the center
Z^{i}(\mathbb{Q})
of
P^{i}(\mathbb{Q})
whose action on
\mathcal{X}_{i}
is trivial, and let
\Gamma_{Z}^{i}
be any arithmetic subgroup of
Z^{i}(\mathbb{Q})^{\circ}
. Denote by
(\Gamma^{i}_{Z})^{-}
the closure of
\Gamma^{i}_{Z}
in
P^{i}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
.
By
[
64
, Lem. 3.7(b)]
,

\varprojlim_{K^{1,p}}\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{1,p}K_{p}^{1}}(P^{1},\mathcal{X}_{1})(\mathbb{C})\cong P^{i}(\mathbb{Q})\backslash\mathcal{X}_{i}\times P^{i}(\mathbb{A}_{f})/(\Gamma^{i}_{Z})^{-}K_{p}^{i}.

Note that the identity component of the center
Z^{i}
of
P^{i}
is a torus, since the Lie algebra
\mathrm{Lie}\ W_{i}
of the unipotent radical
W^{i}
of
P^{i}
has nontrivial weight by
[
64
, Def. 2.1]
and
G^{i}:=P^{i}/W^{i}
is reductive. By a theorem of Chevalley (see
[
11
, Thm. 1]
and
[
20
, 2.0.10]
), any finite index subgroup of the group of units
U^{i}
of
Z^{i}(\mathbb{Q})
is open in
U^{i}
under the topology induced by
P^{i}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
and
P^{i}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
, so
(\Gamma_{Z}^{i})^{-}
is the completion
(\Gamma_{Z}^{i})^{\widehat{}}
with respect to finite index subgroups in
U^{i}
, and
(\Gamma_{Z}^{i})^{-}
is also the closure of
\Gamma_{Z}^{i}
in
P^{i}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
.
Let
\widehat{P}^{i}(\mathbb{Q}):=\varprojlim P^{i}(\mathbb{Q})/U^{i,n}
be the completion with respect to
U^{i,n}
. Note that the image of
Z^{1}(\mathbb{Q})
in
P^{2}(\mathbb{Q})
is contained in the
\mathbb{R}
-points of the centralizer of
\mathbb{S}\xrightarrow{h_{x,\infty}^{1}}P^{1}_{\mathbb{R}}\to P^{2}_{\mathbb{R}}
, which is compact modulo
Z^{2}(\mathbb{R})W^{2}(\mathbb{R})
. Then there is a well-defined morphism
\widehat{P}^{1}(\mathbb{Q})\backslash P^{1}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})\to\widehat{P}^{2}(\mathbb{Q})\backslash P^{2}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
. From the argument in
[
36
, Lem. 2.1.2]
verbatim, the morphism above and the morphism
\mathcal{X}_{1}\times P^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})/K_{p}^{1}\to\mathcal{X}_{2}\times P^{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})/K_{p}^{2}
are injective, and the desired morphism is also injective. Note that the argument of
[
17
, 1.15.3]
used in the argument of
[
36
, Lem. 2.1.2]
also works, if we replace
G^{1}
and
G^{2}
in the proof of
[
17
, 1.15.3]
with
P^{1}
and
P^{2}
, respectively.
∎

Let
(G,\mathcal{X},\hbar)
be a pure Shimura datum. Recall that
G
is a connected reductive group over
\mathbb{Q}
. Assume that
K_{p}
is an open compact subgroup of
G(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
that contains a parahoric group
K_{p}^{\circ}:=\mathscr{G}^{\circ}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
, where
\mathscr{G}^{\circ}
is a parahoric group scheme of
G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
over
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
. Let
K^{p}
be a neat open compact subgroup of
G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
as before. Let ??be the maximal field extension of
E:=E(G,\mathcal{X})
that is unramified at all primes dividing
p
. We record the following lemma:

Proposition 1.23
.

With the assumptions above, the geometrically connected components of
\mathrm{Sh}_{K}:=\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{p}K^{p}}(G,\mathcal{X})
are defined over
E^{p}
.

Proof.

By
[
64
, Prop. 2.11]
,
(G,\mathcal{X})
embeds into a pure Shimura datum
(G_{1},\mathcal{X}_{1})
, which has the same reflex field
E
as
(G,\mathcal{X})
. In fact,
(G_{1},\mathcal{X}_{1}):=(T,\mathcal{Y})\times\hbar(G,\mathcal{X})
, where
(T,\mathcal{Y}):=(G,\mathcal{X})/G^{\mathrm{der}}
is a pure Shimura datum associated with the cocenter
T:=G/G^{\mathrm{der}}
.
Let
K_{1}\subset G_{1}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
be a neat open compact subgroup such that
K_{1}:=K_{1,p}K^{p}_{1}
with
K_{1,p}\subset G_{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
of the form
K_{1,p}=K_{T,p}\times K_{p}
,
K_{1,p}\cap G(\mathbb{Q}_{p})=K_{p}
, and
K_{1}^{p}
neat open compact in
G_{1}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
.
We can further choose
K_{T,p}\subset T(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
to be the unique parahoric subgroup of
T(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
.
By Lemma
1.22
, there is a neat open compact subgroup
K^{p}_{1}\subset G_{1}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
such that the induced morphism
\mathrm{Sh}_{K}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{1}}
is a closed embedding between smooth varieties of equal dimensions, so it is open and closed. Moreover, this embedding is defined over
E
by
[
64
, Prop. 11.10]
.
Then we are reduced to show the statement for
\hbar(G,\mathcal{X})
and for
(T,\mathcal{Y})
.
For the former one, the proof of
[
33
, Lem. 4.3.2 and Cor. 4.3.9]
works verbatim. For the latter one, note that
(T,\mathcal{Y})
is a pure Shimura datum with
T
a torus and
\mathcal{Y}
finite. In this case, the proof similar to
loc. cit
. also works.
Indeed, by the discussion in §
1.1.2
, the Galois group
\mathrm{Gal}(\overline{E}_{T}/E_{T})
acts on
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{T}}(\overline{E}_{T})
via
r_{E_{T}}(T,\mathcal{Y})/K_{T,p}:\mathrm{Gal}(\overline{E}_{T}/E_{T})\to T(\mathbb{Q})^{+}\backslash T(\mathbb{A}_{f})/K_{T,p}
.
For any place
v|p
of
E_{T}\subset E
, the image of
\mathcal{O}^{\times}_{E_{T,v}}
in
T(\mathbb{Q})^{+}\backslash T(\mathbb{A}_{f})/K_{T,p}
is trivial under
r_{E_{T}}(T,\mathcal{Y})\circ\mathrm{rec}_{E_{T}}^{-1}
, since
K_{T,p}
is the unique Iwahori subgroup of
T(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
, since
\mathcal{O}^{\times}_{E_{T},v}
is the unique Iwahori subgroup of
R_{E_{T,v}/\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\mathbb{G}_{m}
by
[
27
, Lem. 2.5.18]
, and since
r_{E_{T}}(T,\mathcal{Y})\circ\mathrm{rec}_{E_{T}}^{-1}|_{E_{T,v}}
is induced by a homomorphism between algebraic tori

R_{E_{T,v}/\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\mathbb{G}_{m}\xrightarrow{R_{E_{T,v}/\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\mu}R_{E_{T,v}/\mathbb{Q}_{p}}T_{E_{T,v}}\xrightarrow{\mathrm{Norm}_{E_{T,v}/\mathbb{Q}_{p}}}T_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}},

which sends the Iwahori subgroup to the Iwahori subgroup by
[
27
, Lem. 2.5.19]
. This means that
\mathrm{Gal}(\overline{E}_{T}/E_{T}^{p})
acts trivially on
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{T}}(T,\mathcal{Y})_{\overline{E}_{T}}
. Hence, we have the desired statement for both
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{T}}
and
\mathrm{Sh}_{K}(G,\hbar(\mathcal{X}))
and finally for
\mathrm{Sh}_{K}(G,\mathcal{X})
.
∎

1.3.5.

We come back to the setup that
(G,X)
is a Shimura datum. Let
K=K_{p}K^{p}
be an open compact subgroup of
G(\mathbb{A}_{f})
such that
K_{p}\subset G(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
and
K^{p}
is neat open compact in
G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
.
In this subsection, we assume one of the following assumptions:

(1)

G
is quasi-split and unramified at
p
, and
K_{p}=G_{{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
is a hyperspecial subgroup associated with some smooth reductive model
G_{{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}
of
G
over
{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}
.

(2)

K_{p}=\mathscr{G}^{\circ}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
is a parahoric subgroup of
G(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
.

Proposition 1.24
.

With one of the assumptions above,
for any cusp label representative
\Phi
, the geometrically connected components of
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi},h}
,
\overline{\mathrm{Sh}}_{K_{\Phi}}
and
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi}}
are defined over
E^{p}
.

Proof.

It suffices to show that the geometrically connected components of
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi},h}
are defined over
E^{p}
. In the first case,
K_{\Phi,h}
is also hyperspecial; in the second case,
K_{\Phi,h}
is quasi-parahoric by
[
51
, Prop. 2.67]
. Hence, the statement follows from Proposition
1.23
above. In the first case, we can also replace the reference
[
33
, Cor. 4.3.9]
in the proof of the proposition above with
[
36
, Prop. 2.2.4]
.
∎

1.4.
ZP
-cusps

We explain how to construct some cusp labels that are
not
irreducible (see §
1.1.1
for the definition), which will be helpful to our purpose. We do not assume that the Shimura data being considered are of abelian type until §
1.4.4
.

1.4.1.

Let
Z:=Z_{G}
be the center of
G
as before. We denote by
??
the identity component of the group generated by
Z
and
P_{\Phi}
, for any cusp label representative
\Phi
. Note that
ZP_{\Phi}
is also a normal subgroup of
Q_{\Phi}
over
\mathbb{Q}
. The group
ZP_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
acts on cusp label representatives by sending
\Phi=(Q_{\Phi},X^{+}_{\Phi},g_{\Phi})
to
q^{\prime}\Phi:=(Q_{\Phi},X^{+}_{\Phi},q^{\prime}g_{\Phi})
for any
q^{\prime}\in ZP_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
.

Definition 1.25
.

We say an admissible cone decomposition (see Convention
1.17
)
\Sigma
is
ZP
-invariant
if
\Sigma(q^{\prime}\Phi)=\Sigma(\Phi)
for any cusp label representative
\Phi
and for any
q^{\prime}\in ZP_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
.

The following lemma is purely combinatorial and is similar to
[
44
, Prop. 6.3.3.5]
:

Lemma 1.26
.

Let
\Sigma
be an admissible cone decomposition with the property
\mathcal{P}
, where
\mathcal{P}=

\emptyset
, “smooth”, “projective” or “smooth and projective”. Then there is a refinement
\Sigma^{\prime}
of
\Sigma
which is
ZP
-invariant and has the property
\mathcal{P}
.

Definition 1.27
.

For any cusp label representative
\Phi
, we define the
magnitude
of
\Phi
to be the dimension of the torus
\mathbf{E}_{K_{\Phi}}
, denoted by
??
.

Definition 1.28
.

There is a partial order
\preceq
associated with the set of cusp label representatives forming a poset
(\{\Phi\},\preceq)
defined by the following:
\Phi_{1}\preceq\Phi_{2}
if there are
q^{\prime}\in ZP_{\Phi_{2}}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
and
\gamma\in G(\mathbb{Q})
such that

\gamma P_{\Phi_{1}}\gamma^{-1}\subset P_{\Phi_{2}}
,
P_{\Phi_{1}}(\mathbb{Q})\gamma X^{+}_{\Phi_{1}}=P_{\Phi_{2}}(\mathbb{Q})X^{+}_{\Phi_{2}}
and
\gamma g_{\Phi_{1}}\equiv q^{\prime}g_{\Phi_{2}}
modulo
K
, denoted also by
\Phi_{1}\xrightarrow{(\gamma,q^{\prime})_{K}}\Phi_{2}
. We write
\Phi_{1}\sim_{ZP}\Phi_{2}
if
\Phi_{1}\preceq\Phi_{2}
and
\Phi_{2}\preceq\Phi_{1}
. Then “
??
” defines an equivalence relation. We denote by
??
the set of equivalence classes
\mathrm{Cusp}_{K}(G,X)/\sim_{ZP},
called the set of
ZP
-cusp labels
.

Similarly, we can define the set of
ZP
-cusp labels with cones

??
to be the set of
ZP
-equivalence classes of
\mathrm{Cusp}_{K}(G,X,\Sigma)
. Indeed, we fix any pairs
(\Phi_{1},\sigma_{1})
and
(\Phi_{2},\sigma_{2})
such that
\sigma_{i}\in\Sigma(\Phi_{i})
and
\sigma_{i}^{\circ}\subset\mathbf{P}_{\Phi_{i}}^{+}
, for
i=1
and
2
. We define
(\Phi_{1},\sigma_{1})\sim_{ZP}(\Phi_{2},\sigma_{2})
, if there are
\gamma\in G(\mathbb{Q})
and
q^{\prime}\in ZP_{\Phi_{2}}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
, such that
\Phi_{1}\xrightarrow[\sim]{(\gamma,q^{\prime})_{K}}\Phi_{2}
and
\mathrm{int}(\gamma)\sigma_{1}=\sigma_{2}
. Then we can define
\mathrm{Cusp}_{K}^{ZP}(G,X,\Sigma):=\mathrm{Cusp}_{K}(G,X,\Sigma)/\sim_{ZP}
. There is also a partial order on
\mathrm{Cusp}_{K}^{ZP}(G,X,\Sigma)
defined similarly:
[(\Phi_{1},\sigma_{1})]\preceq_{ZP}[(\Phi_{2},\sigma_{2})]
if and only if
\Phi_{1}\xrightarrow{(\gamma,q^{\prime})_{K}}\Phi_{2}
and
\gamma^{-1}\sigma_{2}\gamma
is a face of
\sigma_{1}
.

Lemma 1.29
.

Let
Q
be an admissible
\mathbb{Q}
-parabolic subgroup of
G
, and let
X^{+}
be any connected component of
X
. Let
I^{G,X^{+},ZP}(Q):=\{[\Phi]\in\mathrm{Cusp}_{K}^{ZP}(G,X)|\Phi\sim_{ZP}(Q,X^{+},g_{\Phi}),g_{\Phi}\in G(\mathbb{A}_{f})\}
. Then
I^{G,X^{+},ZP}(Q)\cong\mathrm{Stab}_{Q(\mathbb{Q})}(D_{Q,X^{+}})ZP_{Q}(\mathbb{A}_{f})\backslash G(\mathbb{A}_{f})/K
.

We will omit the superscript
G
and
X^{+}
, and write
I^{G,X^{+},ZP}(Q)
as
I^{ZP}(Q)
, if
G
and
X^{+}
are clear in the context.

Proof of Lemma
1.29
.

This follows verbatim from the proof of Proposition
1.4
, after replacing
P_{Q}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
there with
ZP_{Q}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
here and replacing
\mathrm{Cusp}_{K}(G,X)
there with
\mathrm{Cusp}_{K}^{ZP}(G,X)
here.

\square

Note that
[
64
, Prop. 2.14(a)]
can be strengthened naively.

Lemma 1.30

(
[
64
, Prop. 2.14(a)]
)
.

The conjugation of
ZP_{\Phi}
on
U_{\Phi}
is a scalar multiplication:
c_{\Phi}:ZP_{\Phi}\to\mathbb{G}_{m}
.

Proof.

Since
Z
acts on
U_{\Phi}
trivially, since the quotient of
ZP_{\Phi}
by
Z\cap ZP_{\Phi}
is
P_{\Phi}
, and since
(P_{\Phi},D_{\Phi})
is irreducible, we can reduce our statement to
loc. cit.
by taking the quotient of
Z\cap ZP_{\Phi}
.
∎

Proof of Lemma
1.26
.

Note that we only need to consider one cusp label representative
\Phi
in each equivalence class of the relation
\sim_{ZP}
: In fact,
K^{\prime}_{\Phi,U}
is a scalar multiplication of
q^{\prime}K^{\prime}_{\Phi,U}(q^{\prime})^{-1}
by Lemma
1.30
or the proof of
[
64
, Prop. 2.14 (a)]
for
q^{\prime}\in ZP_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
. Suppose that
q^{\prime}K^{\prime}_{\Phi,U}(q^{\prime})^{-1}=c_{\Phi}(q^{\prime})K^{\prime}_{\Phi,U}
for some
c_{\Phi}(q^{\prime})\in\mathbb{A}_{f}^{\times}
. Since we have the decomposition
\mathbb{A}_{f}^{\times}=\mathbb{Q}^{\times}\cdot\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{\times}
and since
U_{\Phi}
is connected and unipotent,
\mathbf{\Lambda}_{K_{\Phi}}
is a
\mathbb{Q}^{\times}
-scalar multiplication of
\mathbf{\Lambda}_{K_{q^{\prime}\Phi}}
. Therefore,
\Sigma(\Phi)
has property
\mathcal{P}
with respect to
\mathbf{\Lambda}_{K_{\Phi}}
if and only if
\Sigma(\Phi^{\prime})
has the property
\mathcal{P}
with respect to
\mathbf{\Lambda}_{K_{\Phi^{\prime}}}
for any cusp label repressentatives
\Phi
and
\Phi^{\prime}
such that
\Phi\sim_{ZP}\Phi^{\prime}
.

Next, we fix a complete set of representatives
\mathcal{R}
in
\mathcal{CLR}(G,X)
of the equivalence classes
\mathrm{Cusp}_{K}^{ZP}(G,X)
of
ZP
-cusp labels. We prove the statement by induction on the magnitude of cusp label representatives
\Phi\in\mathcal{R}
. If
m(\Phi)=0
, then
\Phi=(G,X^{+},g_{\Phi})
and there is nothing to show since
\mathbf{P}_{\Phi}
is trivial.
Assume that we have refined
\Sigma
by some cone decompositon
\Sigma_{n}
for cusp label representatives
\Phi
of magnitude
m(\Phi)\leq n
with the property
\mathcal{P}
such that the collection
\{\Sigma_{n}(\Phi)\}_{m(\Phi)\leq n}
is invariant under the left action of
ZP_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
.
For any two cusp label representatives
\Phi_{1}\xrightarrow{(\gamma,q)_{K}}\Phi_{2}
and
q^{\prime}\in ZP_{\Phi_{2}}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
,
q^{\prime}\Phi_{1}\xrightarrow{(\gamma,q^{\prime}q(q^{\prime})^{-1})_{K}}q^{\prime}\Phi_{2}
. So for any cusp label representative
\Phi\in\mathcal{R}
of magnitude
m(\Phi)=n+1
, the cone decomposition
\Sigma_{n}
defines equal cone decompositions for
\mathbf{P}_{q^{\prime}\Phi}-\mathbf{P}_{q^{\prime}\Phi}^{+}
for any
q^{\prime}\in ZP_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
.
There is an admissible cone decomposition
\Sigma_{n+1}(\Phi)
for
\mathbf{P}_{\Phi}
with the property
\mathcal{P}

extending
the cone decomposition for
\mathbf{P}_{\Phi}-\mathbf{P}^{+}_{\Phi}
defined by
\Sigma_{n}
, and we can define cone decompositions
\Sigma(q^{\prime}\Phi):=\Sigma(\Phi)
for all
q^{\prime}\in ZP_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
.
Therefore, we can extend the definition of
\Sigma_{n+1}
to all cusp labels of magnitudes
\leq n+1
, then we can get the desired refinement
\Sigma^{\prime}
of
\Sigma
by induction. For the results of extendability of admissible cone decompositions for
\mathbf{P}_{\Phi}-\mathbf{P}^{+}_{\Phi}
to
\mathbf{P}_{\Phi}
preserving the property
\mathcal{P}
, see, e.g.,
[
30
, pp. 32-35]
and
[
64
, 5.20-5.25 and 9.20]
.

\square

Remark 1.31
.

We can see from Lemma
1.29
that a
ZP
-cusp label with cone is determined by a
ZP
-cusp label
[\Phi]
and a
??
-orbit
[\sigma]
in
U_{\Phi}(\mathbb{R})
, where
\Delta_{\Phi,K}^{ZP}:=\mathrm{Stab}_{Q(\mathbb{Q})}(D_{\Phi})\cap ZP(\mathbb{A}_{f})g_{\Phi}Kg_{\Phi}^{-1}/ZP_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})
. We will omit the subscript
K
in
\Delta^{ZP}_{\Phi,K}
if
K
is clear in the context.

Definition 1.32
.

Fix an admissible cone decomposition
\Sigma
. Let
Q
be an admissible
\mathbb{Q}
-parabolic subgroup of
G
, and let
X^{+}
be any connected component of
X
. Define
I^{G,X^{+},ZP}(Q,\Sigma):=\{[(\Phi,\sigma)]\in\mathrm{Cusp}_{K}^{ZP}(G,X,\Sigma)|(\Phi,\sigma)\sim_{ZP}(Q,X^{+},g_{\Phi};\sigma^{\prime}),g_{\Phi}\in G(\mathbb{A}_{f}),\sigma^{\prime}\in\Sigma(Q,X^{+},g_{\Phi})\}
, and similarly define
I^{G,X^{+}}(Q,\Sigma):=\{[(\Phi,\sigma)]\in\mathrm{Cusp}_{K}(G,X,\Sigma)|(\Phi,\sigma)\sim(Q,X^{+},g_{\Phi};\sigma^{\prime}),g_{\Phi}\in G(\mathbb{A}_{f}),\sigma^{\prime}\in\Sigma(Q,X^{+},g_{\Phi})\}
.

We shall omit the superscript “
G,X^{+}
” if it is clear in the context.

We will use the following propositions later:

Proposition 1.33
.

Let
\Phi
be a cusp label representative, and let
\sigma\in\Sigma(\Phi)
. Define

??:=\{[\Phi^{\prime}]\in\mathrm{Cusp}_{K}(G,X)|\Phi^{\prime}\sim_{ZP}\Phi\},

and define

??:=\{[(\Phi^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime})]\in\mathrm{Cusp}_{K}(G,X,\Sigma)|(\Phi^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime})\sim_{ZP}(\Phi,\sigma)\}.

Then
[ZP(\Phi)]
is bijective to
\widetilde{\Delta}^{ZP}_{\Phi,K}\backslash(P_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f})\backslash ZP_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f})/\widetilde{K}_{\Phi})
and
[ZP(\Phi,\sigma)]
is bijective to

\widetilde{\Delta}^{ZP,\circ}_{\Phi,K}\backslash(P_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f})\backslash ZP_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f})/\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}),

where
\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}:=ZP(\mathbb{A}_{f})\cap g_{\Phi}Kg_{\Phi}^{-1}
,
\widetilde{\Delta}_{\Phi,K}^{ZP}:=\mathrm{Stab}_{Q_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})}(D_{\Phi})\cap ZP_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f})g_{\Phi}Kg_{\Phi}^{-1}
and
\widetilde{\Delta}_{\Phi,K}^{ZP,\circ}:=\mathrm{Stab}_{Q_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})}(D_{\Phi},\sigma)\cap ZP_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f})g_{\Phi}Kg_{\Phi}^{-1}.

Proof.

By definition,
[ZP(\Phi)]
is the fiber of
I(Q_{\Phi})\to I^{ZP}(Q_{\Phi})
at
[\Phi]
. This fiber is bijective to the fiber of

\mathrm{Stab}_{Q(\mathbb{Q})}(D_{\Phi})P_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f})\backslash G(\mathbb{A}_{f})/K\to\mathrm{Stab}_{Q(\mathbb{Q})}(D_{\Phi})ZP_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f})\backslash G(\mathbb{A}_{f})/K

at
[g_{\Phi}]
, which is
\widetilde{\Delta}^{ZP}_{\Phi,K}\backslash(P_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f})\backslash ZP_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f})g_{\Phi}K/K)\cong\widetilde{\Delta}_{\Phi,K}^{ZP}\backslash(P_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f})\backslash ZP_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f})/K_{\Phi})
. The other statement can also be proved in a similar way. The set
[ZP(\Phi,\sigma)]
is the fiber of

\mathrm{Stab}_{Q(\mathbb{Q})}(D_{\Phi})P_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f})\backslash\Sigma(\Phi)\times G(\mathbb{A}_{f})/K\to\mathrm{Stab}_{Q(\mathbb{Q})}(D_{\Phi})ZP_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f})\backslash\Sigma(\Phi)\times G(\mathbb{A}_{f})/K

at
(\sigma,g_{\Phi})
, such that
(Q_{\Phi},X^{+}_{\Phi},g_{\Phi};\sigma)
represents
[(\Phi,\sigma)]
. Since the stabilizer in
\mathrm{Stab}_{Q(\mathbb{Q})}(D_{\Phi})
of
P_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f})\backslash\sigma\times ZP_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f})g_{\Phi}K/K
is
\widetilde{\Delta}^{ZP,\circ}_{\Phi,K}
,
[ZP(\Phi,\sigma)]
is bijective to
\widetilde{\Delta}^{ZP,\circ}_{\Phi,K}\backslash(P_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f})\backslash ZP_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f})/K_{\Phi})
.
∎

Let
??:=\widetilde{\Delta}^{ZP,\circ}_{\Phi,K}/ZP_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})
.
Again, we shall omit the subscript
K
in
\widetilde{\Delta}^{ZP}_{\Phi,K}
,
\widetilde{\Delta}^{ZP,\circ}_{\Phi,K}
and
\Delta^{ZP,\circ}_{\Phi,K}
if it is clear in the context.

Proposition 1.34
.

For any
[\Phi^{\prime}]\in[ZP(\Phi)]
, there is a cone
\sigma^{\prime}\in\Sigma(\Phi^{\prime})
such that
[(\Phi^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime})]\in[ZP(\Phi,\sigma)]
.
The fiber of the natural projection
\ell:[ZP(\Phi,\sigma)]\to[ZP(\Phi)]
at
[\Phi^{\prime}]\in[ZP(\Phi)]
is bijective to

\Delta_{\Phi^{\prime}}
-orbits of the
\Delta^{ZP}_{\Phi^{\prime}}
-orbit
[\sigma^{\prime}]_{ZP}
. Moreover, we can choose a representative
\Phi^{\prime\prime}\sim\Phi^{\prime}
such that
\Phi^{\prime\prime}=(Q_{\Phi},X^{+}_{\Phi},g_{\Phi^{\prime\prime}}g_{\Phi})
for some
g_{\Phi^{\prime\prime}}
lifting an element in
\widetilde{\Delta}^{ZP}_{\Phi}\backslash(P_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f})\backslash ZP_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f})/\widetilde{K}_{\Phi})
, then this fiber is bijective to
\Delta_{\Phi}
-orbits of the
\Delta^{ZP}_{\Phi}
-orbit
[\sigma]_{ZP}
.

Proof.

If
\Phi\xrightarrow[\sim]{({\gamma},{q})_{K}}\Phi^{\prime}
for
\gamma\in G(\mathbb{Q})
and for
q\in ZP_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
, then we let
\sigma^{\prime}:=\gamma\sigma
and
(\Phi,\sigma)\xrightarrow[\sim]{({\gamma},{q})_{K}}(\Phi^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime})
. (Here we are using an abbreviation:
\gamma\sigma=\mathrm{int}(\gamma)\sigma
, and the same for the paragraph below.)

For the second statement, we consider the commutative diagram

(1.11)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces I(Q_{\Phi},\Sigma)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces I^{ZP}(Q_{\Phi},\Sigma)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces I(Q_{\Phi})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces I^{ZP}(Q_{\Phi})}

Suppose that
[\Phi^{\prime}]\in I(Q_{\Phi})
maps to
[\Phi]\in I^{ZP}(Q_{\Phi})
. The fiber of
[\Phi^{\prime}]
along
I(Q_{\Phi},\Sigma)\to I(Q_{\Phi})
is
\{([\Phi^{\prime}],[\sigma^{\prime\prime}])|[\sigma^{\prime\prime}]\in\Delta_{\Phi^{\prime}}\backslash\Sigma(\Phi^{\prime})\}
. We still assume that
\Phi\xrightarrow[\sim]{({\gamma},{q})_{K}}\Phi^{\prime}
. Then
([\Phi^{\prime}],[\sigma^{\prime\prime}])
maps to
([\Phi],[\gamma^{-1}\sigma^{\prime\prime}]_{ZP})
via
I(Q_{\Phi},\Sigma)\to I^{ZP}(Q_{\Phi},\Sigma)
, where
[\gamma^{-1}\sigma^{\prime\prime}]_{ZP}
is the
\Delta^{ZP}_{\Phi}
-orbit of
\gamma^{-1}\sigma^{\prime\prime}
. Then the fiber of
\ell
at
[\Phi^{\prime}]
is isomorphic to the elements of
\Delta_{\Phi^{\prime}}\backslash\Sigma(\Phi^{\prime})
that map to
[\sigma]_{ZP}
via

(1.12)

\begin{split}\Delta_{\Phi^{\prime}}\backslash\Sigma(\Phi^{\prime})\longrightarrow\Delta_{\Phi}^{ZP}\backslash\Sigma(\Phi^{\prime})\\
[\sigma^{\prime\prime}]\mapsto[\gamma^{-1}\sigma^{\prime\prime}]_{ZP},\end{split}

which is the pullback of
[\sigma]_{ZP}
along the map above. So it is a
\gamma\Delta^{ZP}_{\Phi}\gamma^{-1}
-orbit of
\sigma^{\prime}=\gamma\sigma
modulo the action of
\Delta_{\Phi^{\prime}}
. Since
\gamma\Delta^{ZP}_{\Phi}\gamma^{-1}=\Delta^{ZP}_{\Phi^{\prime}}
, the fiber of
\ell
at
[\Phi^{\prime}]
is bijective to
\Delta_{\Phi^{\prime}}
-orbits of
[\sigma^{\prime}]_{ZP}
.

The last statement follows from Proposition
1.33
.
∎

1.4.2.

A
ZP
-invariant cone decomposition
\Sigma
can extend over certain central extensions. We have the following variant of Lemma
1.22
:

Proposition 1.35
.

Let
G_{1}
be a connected reductive group over
\mathbb{Q}
with
Z_{1}
its center. Let
Z_{2}
be a
\mathbb{Q}
-torus containing
Z_{1}
and let
G_{2}:=G_{1}\times^{Z_{1}}Z_{2}
be a connected reductive group with center
Z_{2}
. Denote by
\iota:G_{1}\to G_{2}
the embedding induced by the inclusion
Z_{1}\hookrightarrow Z_{2}
.
We abusively denote by
\iota:(G_{1},X_{1})\hookrightarrow(G_{2},X_{2})
the embedding of Shimura data induced by
\iota:G_{1}\to G_{2}
.
Let
K_{1}
be an open compact subgroup of
G_{1}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
of the form
K_{1}=K_{1,p}K_{1}^{p}
where

K_{1,p}
is open compact and
K_{1}^{p}\subset G_{1}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
is neat open compact. Then:

(1)

There is a
\mathbb{Q}
-subgroup
Z_{3}
of
Z_{2}
of multiplicative type such that
Z_{2}=Z_{1}\cdot Z_{3}
and
Z_{1}\cap Z_{3}
is finite.

(2)

Let
K_{2}:=K_{2,p}K^{p}_{2}
. There is a neat open compact subgroup
K_{2}^{p,\prime}\subset G_{2}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
such that, for any neat open compact
K_{2}^{p}\subset K_{2}^{p,\prime}
and
K_{2}\cap G_{1}(\mathbb{A}_{f})=K_{1}
, the following statements are true:
\iota
induces an open and closed embedding of Shimura varieties

\iota:\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{1}}(G_{1},X_{1})\hookrightarrow\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{2}}(G_{2},X_{2});

moreover, let
\Phi_{1}
be any cusp label representative of
(G_{1},X_{1})
which maps to a cusp label representative
\Phi_{2}
of
(G_{2},X_{2})
via
\iota
, we have isomorphisms

U_{\Phi_{1}}\cong U_{\Phi_{2}}
,
\mathbf{\Lambda}_{K_{\Phi_{1}}}\cong\mathbf{\Lambda}_{K_{\Phi_{2}}}
, and

\iota_{\Phi_{1}}:\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi_{1}}}(P_{\Phi_{1}},D_{\Phi_{1}})\to\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi_{2}}}(P_{\Phi_{2}},D_{\Phi_{2}}).

(3)

If
Z_{3}^{\circ}
, the identity component of
Z_{3}
, is isogenous to a product of
\mathbb{Q}
-split tori and compact-type tori, then the results in the last part hold for any neat open compact
K_{2}
.

Proof.

In fact, there is a
\mathbb{Q}
-subtori
T_{3}
of
\overline{Z}_{2}:=Z_{2}/Z_{G_{1}^{\mathrm{der}}}
such that the intersection of
\overline{Z}_{1}:=Z_{1}/Z_{G_{1}^{\mathrm{der}}}
and
T_{3}
is finite, and such that
\overline{Z}_{2}=\overline{Z_{1}}\cdot T_{3}
. We then find such a
Z_{3}
by taking
Z_{3}:=Z_{2}\times_{\overline{Z}_{2}}T_{3}
.

The first statement in the second part follows directly from
[
36
, Lem. 2.1.2]
.
Since
G_{1}
is a normal subgroup of
G_{2}
and
G_{1}^{\mathrm{der}}=G_{2}^{\mathrm{der}}
,
P_{\Phi_{1}}\cong P_{\Phi_{2}}
,
U_{\Phi_{1}}\cong U_{\Phi_{2}}
and
D_{\Phi_{1}}\cong D_{\Phi_{2}}
by definition. Since
K_{\Phi_{1}}=P_{\Phi_{1}}(\mathbb{A}_{f})\cap g_{\Phi_{1}}K_{1}g_{\Phi_{1}}^{-1}=P_{\Phi_{2}}(\mathbb{A}_{f})\cap g_{\Phi_{1}}K_{2}g_{\Phi_{1}}^{-1}\cap G_{1}(\mathbb{A}_{f})=P_{\Phi_{2}}(\mathbb{A}_{f})\cap g_{\Phi_{1}}K_{2}g_{\Phi_{1}}^{-1}=K_{\Phi_{2}}
, we have isomorphisms between boundary mixed Shimura varieties. Moreover,
\mathbf{\Lambda}_{K_{\Phi_{1}}}\subset\mathbf{\Lambda}_{K_{\Phi_{2}}}
is of finite index, we can choose
K_{2}
such that
K_{2}/K_{1}
sufficiently small and
\mathbf{\Lambda}_{K_{\Phi_{1}}}=\mathbf{\Lambda}_{K_{\Phi_{2}}}
. Note that this is possible because the number of cusp labels of
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{1}}
is finite and remains unchanged if we shrink
K_{2}
. Since we only need to choose
K_{2}
to be sufficiently small but containing
K_{1}
, we can choose a
K_{2}
of the form
K_{2}=K_{2,p}K_{2}^{p}
.

When
Z_{3}^{\circ}
is isogenous to a product of
\mathbb{Q}
-split tori and compact-type tori, and
K_{2}
is neat,
\Delta_{G_{2}/G_{1},K_{2}}
itself is trivial.
∎

Proposition 1.36
.

With the conventions in Lemma
1.26
and Proposition
1.35
, we suppose that
K_{2}
is chosen as in Proposition
1.35
. Let
\Sigma_{1}
be an admissible cone decomposition for
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{1}}(G_{1},X_{1})
. Then there is an admissible
ZP
-invariant cone decomposition
\Sigma_{2}
for
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{2}}(G_{2},X_{2})
with property
\mathcal{P}
such that
\iota^{*}(\Sigma_{2})
is a refinement of
\Sigma_{1}
. Moreover,
\iota^{*}(\Sigma_{2})
also has the property
\mathcal{P}
.

Proof.

It suffices to find a
\Sigma_{2}
for
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{2}}(G_{2},X_{2})
such that
\iota^{*}(\Sigma_{2})
refines
\Sigma_{1}
, because we can refine
\Sigma_{2}
further to obtain a cone decomposition
\Sigma_{2}^{\prime}
with property
\mathcal{P}
by Lemma
1.26
. The induced cone decomposition
\iota^{*}(\Sigma_{2})
will also have the property
\mathcal{P}
since
U_{\Phi_{1}}\cong U_{\Phi_{2}}
and
\mathbf{\Lambda}_{K_{\Phi_{1}}}\cong\mathbf{\Lambda}_{K_{\Phi_{2}}}
from the choice of
K_{2}
above.
Let
Q_{1}
be an admissible parabolic subgroup of
G_{1}
, then there is a unique admissible parabolic subgroup
Q_{2}
of
G_{2}
containing
Q_{1}
.
Since
Z_{2}\cap G_{1}=Z_{1}
,
Q_{1}=G_{1}\cap Q_{2}
and
P_{Q_{1}}=P_{Q_{2}}\cap G_{1}
, and since our choice of
K_{2}
as in Proposition
1.35
, we see that
I(Q_{1})\hookrightarrow I(Q_{2})
.
We then do induction on the magnitude of cusp label representatives
\Phi_{2}
of
(G_{2},X_{2})
.
Fix any complete set of representatives
\mathcal{R}_{1}
of
\mathrm{Cusp}_{K_{1}}(G_{1},X_{1})
, we can extend
\mathcal{R}_{1}
to a complete set of representatives
\mathcal{R}_{2}
of
\mathrm{Cusp}_{K_{2}}(G_{2},X_{2})
since
I(Q_{1})\hookrightarrow I(Q_{2})
.
Assume that we have an admissible cone decomposition
\Sigma_{2}^{n}
with property
\mathcal{P}
for cusp labels of
(G_{2},X_{2})
with magnitude
\leq n
.
Let
\Phi_{2}
be any cusp label representative in
\mathcal{R}_{2}
of magnitude
n+1
. Then
\Phi_{2}
represents an element
[g_{\Phi_{2}}]\in I(Q_{\Phi_{2}})
. For any
\Phi_{2}^{\prime}\succeq\Phi_{2}
, there are
\gamma\in G(\mathbb{Q})
and
q^{\prime}\in P_{\Phi^{\prime}_{2}}
such that
\Phi_{2}\xrightarrow{(\gamma,q^{\prime})_{K_{2}}}\Phi_{2}^{\prime}
. Let
Q_{\Phi_{2}^{\prime}}^{\gamma}:=\gamma^{-1}Q_{\Phi_{2}^{\prime}}\gamma
. Then
\Phi_{2}^{\prime}
represents the image of
[g_{\Phi_{2}}]
under
I(Q_{\Phi_{2}})\to I(Q^{\gamma}_{\Phi_{2}^{\prime}})
induced by
Q_{\Phi_{2}}\hookrightarrow Q_{\Phi_{2}^{\prime}}^{\gamma}
.
If
\Phi_{2}\in\mathcal{R}_{1}
, then the image of
[g_{\Phi_{2}}]
in
I(Q^{\gamma}_{\Phi_{2}^{\prime}})
lies in
I(Q^{\gamma}_{\Phi_{2}^{\prime}}\cap G_{1})
, the cone decomposition for
\mathbf{P}_{\Phi_{2}}-\mathbf{P}_{\Phi_{2}}^{+}
refines
\Sigma_{1}
by induction hypothesis, and we can choose an admissible cone decomposition for
\Phi_{2}
that refines
\Sigma_{1}(\Phi_{2})
and extends
\Sigma_{2}^{n}|_{\mathbf{P}_{\Phi_{2}}-\mathbf{P}_{\Phi_{2}}^{+}}
; if
\Phi_{2}
is not in
\mathcal{R}_{1}
, we can find an admissible cone decomposition for
\mathbf{P}_{\Phi_{2}}
extending that of
\mathbf{P}_{\Phi_{2}}-\mathbf{P}_{\Phi_{2}}^{+}
. Then we get desired result by induction process.
∎

Let
\iota:(G_{1},X_{1})\to(G_{2},X_{2})
be any morphism between Shimura data. For any
g\in G_{2}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
and
K_{1}\subset K_{2}
, there is a morphism

(1.13)

\iota(g):\mathrm{Sh}_{gK_{1}g^{-1}}(G_{1},X_{1})\to\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{2}}(G_{2},X_{2})

defined over the reflex field of
(G_{1},X_{1})
. Over complex points, it is described by
[(x,g_{1})]\mapsto[(x,g_{1}g)]
.
For any admissible cone decomposition
\Sigma_{2}
for
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{2}}(G_{2},X_{2})
, denote by
g^{*}\Sigma_{2}
the induced cone decomposition for
\mathrm{Sh}_{gK_{1}g^{-1}}(G_{1},X_{1})
by pulling back via
\iota(g)
.

Corollary 1.37
.

Let
\iota:(G_{1},X_{1})\hookrightarrow(G_{2},X_{2})
be the embedding as in Proposition
1.35
. Let
K_{1}
and
K_{2}
be the neat open compact subgroups chosen there.
Let
\Sigma_{2}
be the admissible
ZP
-invariant cone decomposition chosen as in Proposition
1.36
. Choose a set of representatives
\{g_{i}\}_{i\in\mathcal{T}}
of
\mathcal{T}:=G_{2}(\mathbb{Q})G_{1}(\mathbb{A}_{f})\backslash G_{2}(\mathbb{A}_{f})/K_{2}
. As the last paragraph, there are morphisms between Shimura varieties

(1.14)

\iota(g_{i}):\mathrm{Sh}_{g_{i}K_{1}g_{i}^{-1}}(G_{1},X_{1})\to\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{2}}(G_{2},X_{2}),

defined by
[(x,g)]\mapsto[(x,gg_{i})]
over complex points,
which induce morphisms between minimal and
toroidal compactifications

(1.15)

\coprod_{g_{i}\in\mathcal{T}}\iota(g_{i})^{\mathrm{min}}:\coprod_{g_{i}\in\mathcal{T}}\mathrm{Sh}_{g_{i}K_{1}g_{i}^{-1}}^{\mathrm{min}}\to\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{2}}^{\mathrm{min}}

and

(1.16)

\coprod_{g_{i}\in\mathcal{T}}\iota(g_{i})^{\mathrm{tor}}:\coprod_{g_{i}\in\mathcal{T}}\mathrm{Sh}_{g_{i}K_{1}g_{i}^{-1}}^{g_{i}^{*}\Sigma_{2}}\longrightarrow\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{2}}^{\Sigma_{2}}.

In the morphism above,
g_{i}^{*}:=\iota(g_{i})^{*}
. Moreover, the morphisms restricted to
\iota^{\mathrm{min}}=\iota(1)^{\mathrm{min}}
and
\iota^{\mathrm{tor}}=\iota(1)^{\mathrm{tor}}
,
\iota^{\mathrm{min}}:\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{min}}_{K_{1}}\to\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{2}}^{\mathrm{min}}
and

\iota^{\mathrm{tor}}:\mathrm{Sh}^{\iota^{*}\Sigma_{2}}_{K_{1}}\to\mathrm{Sh}^{\Sigma_{2}}_{K_{2}}
, are open and closed embeddings over the reflex field of
(G_{1},X_{1})
.

Proof.

We only need to show the last claim on open and closed embeddings. Define
\Delta_{G_{2}/G_{1},K_{2}}:=G_{2}(\mathbb{Q})\cap G_{1}(\mathbb{A}_{f})K_{2}/G_{1}(\mathbb{Q})
.
From
[
64
, Prop. 7.10]
for trivial cone decompositions and the choice of
K_{2}
in Proposition
1.35
, the group
\Delta_{G_{2}/G_{1},K_{2}}
acts on
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{1}}

trivially. Since
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{1}}
is dense in its minimal and toroidal compactifications,
\Delta_{G_{2}/G_{1},K_{2}}
acts on the minimal and toroidal compactifications of
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{1}}
trivially. From
[
64
, Prop. 7.10]
, we see the statement is true for toroidal compactifications. (This can also imply that
\iota=\iota(1)
is an open and closed embedding.) We can check over
\mathbb{C}
to see that the schematic closure
(\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{1}})^{\overline{\ }}
of
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{1}}
in
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{2}}^{\mathrm{min}}
under
\iota
is normal and proper. Since
\iota^{\mathrm{min}}
is proper, quasi-finite, and factors through
(\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{1}})^{\overline{\ }}
, we deduce the claim for minimal compactifications by Zariski’s main theorem.
∎

1.4.3.

Let
G
be a connected reductive group over
\mathbb{Q}
with connected center
Z
. Fix
(G^{\mathrm{ad}},X^{\mathrm{ad}})
, a Shimura datum associated with its adjoint group. Throughout §
1.4.3
, we work under the following assumptions:

•

Suppose that
(G^{\mathrm{ad}},X^{\mathrm{ad}})
lifts to two different Shimura data
(G,X_{a})
and
(G,X_{b})
such that the natural quotient from
G
to
G^{\mathrm{ad}}
induces isomorphisms
(G^{\mathrm{ad}},X_{a}^{\mathrm{ad}})\xrightarrow{\sim}(G^{\mathrm{ad}},X^{\mathrm{ad}})\xleftarrow{\sim}(G^{\mathrm{ad}},X_{b}^{\mathrm{ad}}).
Note that the maps
X_{i}\to X_{i}^{\mathrm{ad}}
are injective for
i=a
and
b
.

•

Suppose that for one (and therefore any)
x\in X^{\mathrm{ad}}
,
h_{x}:\mathbb{S}\to G^{\mathrm{ad}}_{\mathbb{R}}
lifts to unique cocharacters
h^{i}_{x}:\mathbb{S}\to G_{\mathbb{R}}
in
X_{i}
.

Then
c:=h_{x}^{a}\cdot(h_{x}^{b})^{-1}
is a homomorphism that factors through the center
Z_{\mathbb{R}}
of
G_{\mathbb{R}}
, and therefore it is independent of the choice of
x\in X^{\mathrm{ad}}
.

We fix homomorphisms
h_{0}
and
h_{\infty}
as in §
1.1.3
. Since
(H_{0}/H_{0}^{\mathrm{der}})_{\mathbb{C}}\cong\mathbb{S}_{\mathbb{C}}
, we can define
u^{c}:H_{0,\mathbb{C}}\to G_{\mathbb{C}}
by
H_{0,\mathbb{C}}\twoheadrightarrow(H_{0}/H_{0}^{\mathrm{der}})_{\mathbb{C}}\xrightarrow{c_{\mathbb{C}}}Z_{\mathbb{C}}\hookrightarrow G_{\mathbb{C}}
. For
i=a
and
b
, and for any admissible
\mathbb{Q}
-parabolic subgroup
Q
, let
u^{Q,i}_{x}:H_{0,\mathbb{C}}\to G_{\mathbb{C}}
be the unique homomorphisms such that
u^{Q,i}_{x}\circ h_{0}=h_{x}^{i}
, which satisfy
[
64
, Prop. 4.6 (b)]
.

Lemma 1.38
.

u^{Q,a}_{x}=u^{Q,b}_{x}\cdot u^{c}
.

Note that the multiplication in the equation above makes sense because
u^{c}
factors through
Z_{\mathbb{C}}
.

Proof of Lemma
1.38
.

The statement is proved by verifying conditions in
[
64
, Prop. 4.6 (b)]
. Let
v^{Q,a}_{x}:=u^{Q,b}_{x}\cdot u^{c}
. The homomorphism
\pi^{\prime}\circ v^{Q,a}_{x}:H_{0,\mathbb{C}}\to Q_{\mathbb{C}}\twoheadrightarrow(Q/U_{Q})_{\mathbb{C}}
is defined over
\mathbb{R}
since
u^{c}
is defined over
\mathbb{R}
. Since
\omega^{Q,b}_{x,\infty}
is of the form
\omega_{x}^{b}\cdot\lambda
for some cocharacter
\lambda
depending only on
Q
as
[
64
, Prop. 4.6 (b)iii]
and
u^{c}
factors through
Z_{\mathbb{C}}
, we have
\omega^{Q,b}_{x,\infty}\cdot(u^{c}\circ\omega_{0})=\omega^{b}_{x}\cdot(u^{c}\circ\omega_{0})\cdot\lambda
, whose adjoint action on
(\lie{G})_{\mathbb{C}}
is identical to that of
\omega_{x,\infty}^{Q,b}
. So
v^{Q,a}_{x}
satisfies conditions in
[
64
, Prop. 4.6 (b)]
. Hence, the statement follows by the uniqueness part of
loc. cit
.

\square

For
i=a,b
, the map
X_{i}\to X^{\mathrm{ad}}
is injective and sends connected components to connected components. So for any cusp label representative
\Phi
, the connected component
X_{a,\Phi}^{+}
of
X_{a}
associated with
\Phi
corresponds to a connected component
X_{b,\Phi}^{+}
of
X_{b}
; we denote them by
X_{\Phi}^{+}
abusively. So there is a natural one-to-one correspondence between cusp label representatives of
(G,X_{a})
and those of
(G,X_{b})
, sending each of three data identically. Hence, any cone decomposition
\Sigma
for
(G,X_{a})
can also be viewed as a cone decomposition for
(G,X_{b})
. We can abusively denote by
\Phi
a cusp label representative of both
(G,X_{a})
and
(G,X_{b})
.

Proposition 1.39
.

Fix a neat open compact subgroup
K\subset G(\mathbb{A}_{f})
. With the conventions in Lemma
1.26
, let
\Sigma
be any admissible
ZP
-invariant cone decomposition for
(G,X_{a},K)
with property
\mathcal{P}
. Then
\Sigma
also defines an admissible
ZP
-invariant cone decomposition for
(G,X_{b},K)
with property
\mathcal{P}
.

Proof.

By Lemma
1.14
, the definition of smoothness or projectivity does not depend on the center of
P_{\Phi}
, so
\Sigma
also defines a cone decomposition for
(G,X_{b})
with property
\mathcal{P}
. Fix an admissible
\mathbb{Q}
-parabolic subgroup and a connected component
X^{+}
. By Lemma
1.29
,
we have
I_{ZP}(Q)\cong\mathrm{Stab}_{Q(\mathbb{Q})}(D_{Q,X^{+}})ZP_{Q}(\mathbb{A}_{f})\backslash G(\mathbb{A}_{f})/K
. Then the rest of the statement follows from Lemma
1.40
below.
∎

Lemma 1.40
.

Let
P_{\Phi}^{i}
be the smallest normal subgroup of
Q
that
u_{x}^{Q,i}\circ h_{\infty}
factors through, for
i=a,b
. Then
ZP_{\Phi}^{a}=ZP_{\Phi}^{b}
. In particular,
ZP_{\Phi}^{a}(\mathbb{A}_{f})=ZP_{\Phi}^{b}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
.

Proof.

Let
m:Z\times Q\to Q
be the multiplication homomorphism of
Z\times Q
. Since
m\circ(u^{c}\times u^{Q,b}_{x})\circ h_{\infty}=u^{Q,a}_{x}\circ h_{\infty}
by Lemma
1.38
, and
ZP_{\Phi}^{b}
is a normal subgroup of
Q
, we know
ZP_{\Phi}^{b}
contains
P^{a}_{\Phi}
. Hence,
ZP_{\Phi}^{a}\subset ZP_{\Phi}^{b}
. Symmetrically, we can get the opposite direction of inclusion, so the desired statement is proved.
∎

Let ??be the field of definition of the Hodge cocharacter
\mu^{c}
associated with the homomorphism
c
. Note that
(Z,\{c\})
can be viewed as a Shimura datum whose associated Shimura varieties are
0
-dimensional. Let
E_{i}:=E(G,X_{i})
be the reflex field of
(G,X_{i})
. Let
E^{*}
be a field extension of
\mathbb{Q}
such that all geometrically connected components of
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi_{a}}}
and
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi_{b}}}
are defined over
E^{*}
for all cusp label representatives
\Phi_{a}
and
\Phi_{b}
of
(G,X_{a})
and
(G,X_{b})
. We have
E^{c}\subset\widetilde{E}:=E_{a}\cdot E_{b}.

Let
K_{Z}:=K_{Z,p}K^{p}_{Z}
be a neat open compact subgroup in
Z(\mathbb{A}_{f})
, where
K_{Z,p}\subset Z(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\cap K_{p}
and
K_{Z}^{p}\subset Z(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})\cap K^{p}
is neat open compact. We can consider the zero-dimensional Shimura variety
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{Z}}
associated with
(Z,\{c\})
over
E^{c}
. Over complex points,
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{Z}}(\mathbb{C})=Z(\mathbb{Q})\backslash\{c\}\times Z(\mathbb{A}_{f})/K_{Z}
.

Denote by ??a finite field extension of
E^{c}
such that all geometrically connected components of
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{Z}}
are defined over
F_{K_{Z}}
. The base change
(\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{Z}})_{F_{K_{Z}}}
is a disjoint union of finite points over
F_{K_{Z}}
; in particular,
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{Z}}
is proper.

Let
S
be the smallest
\mathbb{Q}
-subgroup of
Z
that
c
factors through. Then the set of cusp labels is represented by
\mathrm{Cusp}_{K_{Z}}(Z,\{c\})\cong Z(\mathbb{Q})S(\mathbb{A}_{f})\backslash Z(\mathbb{A}_{f})/K_{Z}
. For any
z\in Z(\mathbb{A}_{f})
, we can therefore associate a cusp label
[z]
in the double coset above.

Let

m^{\prime}:Z\times G\to Z\times G

be the isomorphism induced by
(z,g)\mapsto(z,zg)
on
R
-value points, where
R
is any
\mathbb{Q}
-algebra.

Proposition 1.41
.

Let
i=a
or
b
. Let
\mathrm{Sh}_{K}^{i,\mathrm{min}}:=\mathrm{Sh}_{K}^{\mathrm{min}}(G,X_{i})
be the minimal compactification of
\mathrm{Sh}_{K}^{i}:=\mathrm{Sh}_{K}(G,X_{i})
. With the conventions above, we have the following statements:

(1)

The isomorphism
m^{\prime}
induces an isomorphism
[m^{\prime}]:\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{Z}}\times\mathrm{Sh}^{b}_{K}\to\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{Z}}\times\mathrm{Sh}^{a}_{K}
over
\widetilde{E}
. Moreover,
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{Z}}\times\mathrm{Sh}_{K}^{i,\mathrm{min}}\cong(\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{Z}}\times\mathrm{Sh}_{K}^{i})^{\mathrm{min}}
, and
[m^{\prime}]
extends to an isomorphism between minimal compactifications

[m^{\prime}]^{\mathrm{min}}:\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{Z}}\times\mathrm{Sh}^{b,\mathrm{min}}_{K}\to\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{Z}}\times\mathrm{Sh}^{a,\mathrm{min}}_{K}
over ??.

(2)

Define

[ZP^{i}(\Phi)]:=\{[\Phi^{\prime}]\in\mathrm{Cusp}_{K}(G,X_{i})|\Phi^{\prime}\sim_{ZP}\Phi\}.

Let
\mathrm{Z}_{[ZP^{i}(\Phi)],K}:=\coprod_{[\Phi^{\prime}]\in[ZP^{i}(\Phi)]}\mathrm{Z}_{[\Phi^{\prime}],K}
be the disjoint union of strata in the minimal compactification corresponding to any cusp label
[\Phi^{\prime}]
in
[ZP^{i}(\Phi)]
. Then

(1.17)

\mathrm{Z}_{[ZP^{i}(\Phi)],K}\cong\widetilde{\Delta}^{ZP}_{\Phi,K}\backslash\mathrm{Sh}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi,h}^{i}}(ZP_{\Phi,h}^{i},ZP_{\Phi,h}^{i}(\mathbb{Q})D_{\Phi,h}),

where
\widetilde{\Delta}^{ZP}_{\Phi,K}:=\mathrm{Stab}_{Q_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})}(D_{\Phi})\cap ZP^{i}_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f})g_{\Phi}Kg_{\Phi}^{-1},
where

\widetilde{K}^{i}_{\Phi,h}:=(ZP^{i}(\mathbb{A}_{f})\cap g_{\Phi}Kg_{\Phi}^{-1})W_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f})/W_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f})

and where
ZP^{i}_{\Phi,h}:=ZP^{i}_{\Phi}/W_{\Phi}
.

(3)

m^{\prime}:Z\times ZP^{b}_{\Phi}\to Z\times ZP^{a}_{\Phi}
induces an isomorphism
[m^{\prime}]^{\Phi}:\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{Z}}\times\mathrm{Z}_{[ZP^{b}(\Phi)],K}\to\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{Z}}\times\mathrm{Z}_{[ZP^{a}(\Phi)],K}
over
\widetilde{E}
.

Proof.

In the proof, we omit the subscripts of the level groups in the symbols of strata and delta groups to simplify the notation. All fiber products are over
\widetilde{E}
if not specified.
By
[
64
, 12.3]
, the projections from
\widetilde{\mathrm{Sh}}^{i}:=\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{Z}}\times\mathrm{Sh}^{i}_{K}
to the first and the second factors induce corresponding morphisms between minimal compactifications
\pi_{1}^{\mathrm{min}}:\widetilde{\mathrm{Sh}}^{i,\mathrm{min}}:=(\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{Z}}\times\mathrm{Sh}^{i}_{K})^{\mathrm{min}}\to\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{Z}}
and
\pi_{2}^{\mathrm{min}}:\widetilde{\mathrm{Sh}}^{i,\mathrm{min}}\to\mathrm{Sh}^{i,\mathrm{min}}_{K}
.
So there is a canonical morphism
\mathrm{id}^{\mathrm{min}}:\widetilde{\mathrm{Sh}}^{i,\mathrm{min}}\to\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{Z}}\times\mathrm{Sh}_{K}^{i,\mathrm{min}}
induced by the universal property of the fiber product. The morphism
\mathrm{id}^{\mathrm{min}}|_{\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{Z}}\times\mathrm{Sh}_{K}^{i}}
is an isomorphism, and
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{Z}}\times\mathrm{Sh}_{K}^{i,\mathrm{min}}
is normal since
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{Z}}
is smooth over
\widetilde{E}
.

Any cusp label
[\Phi^{\times}]
of
\widetilde{\mathrm{Sh}}^{i,\mathrm{min}}
maps to a cusp label
[z]
of
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{Z}}
and also to a cusp label of
\mathrm{Sh}_{K}^{i,\mathrm{min}}
, so there is a canonical morphism
\pi_{[\Phi^{\times}]}:\mathrm{Z}_{[\Phi^{\times}]}\to\mathrm{Z}_{[z]}\times\mathrm{Z}_{[\Phi]}
for some
[z]\in\mathrm{Cusp}_{K_{Z}}(Z,\{c\})
and some
[\Phi]\in\mathrm{Cusp}_{K}(G,X_{i})
fitting into the following diagram

(1.18)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi^{\times},h}}(P_{\Phi^{\times},h},D_{\Phi^{\times},h})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Sh}_{[z]}\times\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi,h}}(P_{\Phi,h},D_{\Phi,h})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Z}_{[\Phi^{\times}]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Z}_{[z]}\times\mathrm{Z}_{[\Phi]}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\pi}_{[\Phi^{\times}]}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{[\Phi^{\times}]}}

In (
1.18
) above, the morphism
\widetilde{\pi}_{[\Phi^{\times}]}
is locally quasi-finite: In fact,
P_{\Phi^{\times}}\hookrightarrow S\times P_{\Phi}
is a closed embedding, so is the morphism between their Levi parts
P_{\Phi^{\times},h}\hookrightarrow S\times P_{\Phi,h}
, then the locally quasi-finiteness follows from
[
64
, Prop. 3.8]
. Moreover, the two vertical morphisms in (
1.18
) are finite, because
\Delta_{\Phi^{\times}}
(resp.
\Delta_{\Phi}
and resp.
\Delta_{[z]}
) acts on
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi^{\times}},h}(P_{\Phi^{\times},h},D_{\Phi^{\times},h})
(resp.
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi},h}(P_{\Phi,h},D_{\Phi,h})
and resp.
\mathrm{Sh}_{[z]}
) through a finite group (resp. through a finite group and resp. trivially). Then
\pi_{[\Phi^{\times}]}
is locally quasi-finite (see
[
71
,
Tag 0GWS
]
).
So the first part is deduced from Zariski’s main theorem, because
\mathrm{id}^{\mathrm{min}}:\widetilde{\mathrm{Sh}}^{i,\mathrm{min}}\to\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{Z}}\times\mathrm{Sh}_{K}^{i,\mathrm{min}}
is a proper birational quasi-finite morphism between normal schemes, and from
[
64
, 12.3]
again.

By §
1.3.1
,

(1.19)

\mathrm{Sh}_{\widetilde{K}^{i}_{\Phi}}(ZP^{i}_{\Phi,h},ZP^{i}_{\Phi,h}(\mathbb{Q})D_{\Phi,h})\cong\coprod_{\Phi^{\prime}}\widetilde{\Delta}_{ZP,\Phi^{\prime},h}\backslash\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi^{\prime}},h}^{i}.

In the equation (
1.19
) above,
\Phi^{\prime}
are cusp label representatives of
(G,X_{i})
, the varieties
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi^{\prime}},h}^{i}
denote the boundary mixed Shimura varieties associated with
(P_{\Phi^{\prime},h}^{i},D_{\Phi^{\prime},h})
and
K_{\Phi^{\prime},h}
, and the disjoint union runs over a set of representatives for

\mathrm{Stab}_{ZP^{i}_{\Phi,h}(\mathbb{Q})}(D_{\Phi,h})P^{i}_{\Phi,h}(\mathbb{A}_{f})\backslash ZP^{i}_{\Phi,h}(\mathbb{A}_{f})/\widetilde{K}^{i}_{\Phi,h};

by strong approximation theorem for unipotent groups (see
[
66
, Lem. 2.7 and Prop. 6.6]
), the double coset above is isomorphic to

(1.20)

\mathrm{Stab}_{ZP^{i}_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})}(D_{\Phi})P^{i}_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f})\backslash ZP^{i}_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f})/\widetilde{K}^{i}_{\Phi},

which represents a set of cusp label representatives
\Phi^{\prime}=(Q_{\Phi},X^{+}_{\Phi},q_{\Phi^{\prime}}g_{\Phi})
for
q_{\Phi^{\prime}}
consisting of a set of representatives for (
1.20
).
Also, in the equation (
1.19
) above,
\widetilde{\Delta}_{ZP,\Phi^{\prime},h}:=\mathrm{Stab}_{ZP^{i}_{\Phi,h}(\mathbb{Q})}(D_{\Phi,h})\cap P_{\Phi^{\prime},h}^{i}(\mathbb{A}_{f})(q_{\Phi^{\prime}}\widetilde{K}^{i}_{\Phi}q_{\Phi^{\prime}}^{-1}W_{\Phi^{\prime}}(\mathbb{A}_{f})/W_{\Phi^{\prime}}(\mathbb{A}_{f}))
, and also for the same reason as the last sentence, the action of
\widetilde{\Delta}_{ZP,\Phi^{\prime}}:=\mathrm{Stab}_{ZP^{i}_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})}(D_{\Phi})\cap P_{\Phi^{\prime}}^{i}(\mathbb{A}_{f})q_{\Phi^{\prime}}\widetilde{K}^{i}_{\Phi}q_{\Phi^{\prime}}^{-1}
on
\mathrm{Sh}^{i}_{K,h}(\Phi^{\prime})
factors surjectively through
\widetilde{\Delta}_{ZP,\Phi^{\prime},h}
.

The quotient of (
1.20
) by
\widetilde{\Delta}^{ZP}_{\Phi}
is

(1.21)

\widetilde{\Delta}^{ZP}_{\Phi}P^{i}_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f})\backslash ZP^{i}_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f})/\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}^{i},

which is in bijection with
[ZP^{i}(\Phi)]
by Proposition
1.33
. The stabilizer in
\mathrm{Stab}_{Q(\mathbb{Q})}(D_{\Phi})
of any
\Phi^{\prime}
represented in the double coset (
1.20
) is
\widetilde{\Delta}_{\Phi^{\prime}}
.
Hence, the quotient by
\widetilde{\Delta}_{\Phi}^{ZP}
of the right-hand side of (
1.19
) can be written as

(1.22)

\coprod\limits_{[\Phi^{\prime}]\in[ZP^{i}(\Phi)]}\Delta_{\Phi^{\prime}}\widetilde{\Delta}_{ZP,\Phi^{\prime}}\backslash\mathrm{Sh}^{i}_{K_{\Phi^{\prime}},h}.

Since
\Delta_{ZP,\Phi^{\prime}}:=\widetilde{\Delta}_{ZP,\Phi^{\prime}}/P^{i}_{\Phi^{\prime}}(\mathbb{Q})\subset\Delta_{\Phi^{\prime}}
, the second part follows.

For the last part, it suffices to check over complex points that if two points on
\mathrm{Sh}_{\widetilde{K}^{i}_{\Phi,h}}(ZP^{i}_{\Phi,h},ZP^{i}_{\Phi,h}(\mathbb{Q})D_{\Phi,h})
are equivalent under the relation defined by the action of
\widetilde{\Delta}^{ZP}_{\Phi}
, then they remain so after the action of any
[z]\in\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{Z}}(\mathbb{C})
. This can be verified since
Z
is the center of
G
.
∎

Lemma 1.42
.

With the conventions above, for any cusp label representative
\Phi
and any
\sigma\in\Sigma(\Phi)
such that
\sigma^{\circ}\subset\mathbf{P}^{+}_{\Phi}
, we have the following statements:

(1)

Let
\mathrm{Stab}_{Q_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})}(D_{\Phi},\sigma)
be the intersection
\mathrm{Stab}_{Q_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})}(D_{\Phi})\cap\mathrm{Stab}_{Q_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})}(\sigma)
. Define
\widetilde{\Delta}^{ZP,\circ}_{\Phi,K}:=\mathrm{Stab}_{Q_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})}(D_{\Phi},\sigma)\cap ZP_{\Phi}^{i}(\mathbb{A}_{f})g_{\Phi}Kg_{\Phi}^{-1}
, which is independent of the value of
i
. Define

[ZP^{i}(\Phi,\sigma)]:=\{(\Phi^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime})\in\mathrm{Cusp}_{K}(G,X_{i},\Sigma)|(\Phi^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime})\sim_{ZP}(\Phi,\sigma)\}.

Then

(1.23)

\coprod\limits_{[(\Phi^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime})]\in[ZP^{i}(\Phi,\sigma)]}\Delta^{i,\circ}_{\Phi^{\prime},K}\backslash\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{i}_{\Phi^{\prime}}}(\sigma^{\prime})\cong\widetilde{\Delta}^{ZP,\circ}_{\Phi,K}\backslash\mathrm{Sh}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}^{i}}(ZP_{\Phi}^{i},ZP^{i}_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})D_{\Phi},\sigma)

over
\widetilde{E}
, where
K_{\Phi^{\prime}}^{i}:=P^{i}(\mathbb{A}_{f})\cap g_{\Phi^{\prime}}Kg_{\Phi^{\prime}}^{-1}
and
\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}^{i}:=ZP^{i}(\mathbb{A}_{f})\cap g_{\Phi}Kg_{\Phi}^{-1}
.

(2)

The isomorphism (
1.23
) can be written as

(1.24)

\coprod\limits_{[\Phi^{\prime}]\in[ZP^{i}(\Phi)]}\coprod\limits_{\delta\in\Delta^{i}_{\Phi^{\prime}}\backslash\Delta^{ZP}_{\Phi^{\prime},K}}\Delta^{i}_{\Phi^{\prime},K}\backslash\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi^{\prime}}}^{i}(\Delta^{i}_{\Phi^{\prime},K}\delta\sigma^{\prime})\cong\Delta^{ZP}_{\Phi,K}\backslash\mathrm{Sh}_{\widetilde{K}^{i}_{\Phi}}(ZP^{i}_{\Phi},ZP^{i}_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})D_{\Phi},\Delta^{ZP}_{\Phi,K}\sigma).

Proof.

Again, we omit the subscripts of the level groups in the symbols of strata and delta groups to simplify the notation.
For the first part, its proof is similar to that of the third part of Proposition
1.41
. Firstly,
\widetilde{\Delta}^{ZP,\circ}_{\Phi}\backslash\mathrm{Sh}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}^{i}}(ZP_{\Phi}^{i},ZP_{\Phi}^{i}(\mathbb{Q})D_{\Phi},\sigma)
is isomorphic to

(1.25)

\widetilde{\Delta}^{ZP,\circ}_{\Phi}\backslash(\coprod_{\Phi^{\prime}}\Delta_{ZP,\Phi^{\prime}}\backslash\mathrm{Sh}^{i}_{K_{\Phi^{\prime}}}(\sigma)).

In (
1.25
) above, the disjoint union runs over a set of cusp label representatives
\Phi^{\prime}=(Q_{\Phi},X_{\Phi}^{+},q_{\Phi^{\prime}}g_{\Phi})
, where
q_{\Phi^{\prime}}
consisting of a set of representatives for

(1.26)

\mathrm{Stab}_{ZP^{i}_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})}(D_{\Phi})P^{i}_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f})\backslash ZP^{i}_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f})/\widetilde{K}^{i}_{\Phi}.

In fact, by §
1.3.1
, we have the following commutative diagram

(1.27)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Sh}_{\widetilde{K}^{i}_{\Phi}}(ZP^{i}_{\Phi},ZP^{i}_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})D_{\Phi})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\coprod_{\Phi^{\prime}}\Delta_{ZP,\Phi^{\prime}}\backslash\mathrm{Sh}^{i}_{K_{\Phi^{\prime}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Sh}_{\overline{\widetilde{K}}^{i}_{\Phi}}(ZP^{i}_{\Phi}/U_{\Phi},ZP^{i}_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})\overline{D}_{\Phi})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\coprod_{\Phi^{\prime}}\Delta_{ZP,\Phi^{\prime}}\backslash\overline{\mathrm{Sh}}^{i}_{K_{\Phi^{\prime}}}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}

Then the corresponding twisted affine torus embedding with respect to
\sigma
of the two vertical morphisms of (
1.27
) are isomorphic.

Since
ZP^{i}_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})
acts on
\sigma
via a scalar multiplication by
[
64
, Prop. 2.14 (a)]
, we know
\mathrm{Stab}_{ZP^{i}_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})}(D_{\Phi})\subset\mathrm{Stab}_{Q(\mathbb{Q})}(D_{\Phi},\sigma)
. So the quotient of (
1.26
