) by
\widetilde{\Delta}_{\Phi}^{ZP,\circ}
is

(1.28)

\widetilde{\Delta}^{ZP,\circ}_{\Phi}P^{i}_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f})\backslash ZP^{i}_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f})/\widetilde{K}^{i}_{\Phi},

which is in bijection with
[ZP^{i}(\Phi,\sigma)]
by Proposition
1.33
.
The stabilizer in
\mathrm{Stab}_{Q(\mathbb{Q})}(D_{\Phi},\sigma)
of any
\Phi^{\prime}
represented in the double coset (
1.26
) is
\widetilde{\Delta}^{\circ}_{\Phi^{\prime}}
.
Hence, the quotient (
1.25
) can be written as

(1.29)

\coprod\limits_{[(\Phi^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime})]\in[ZP^{i}(\Phi,\sigma)]}\Delta^{\circ}_{\Phi^{\prime}}\Delta_{ZP,\Phi^{\prime}}\backslash\mathrm{Sh}^{i}_{K_{\Phi^{\prime}}}(\sigma).

Since
\Delta_{ZP,\Phi^{\prime}}\subset\Delta^{\circ}_{\Phi^{\prime}}
by
[
64
, Prop. 2.14 (a)]
again, the desired result for representatives as in (
1.26
) is proved. For another choice of representatives
(\Phi^{\prime\prime},\sigma^{\prime\prime})
for
[(\Phi^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime})]
, there is an equivalence

(\Phi^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime})\xrightarrow[\sim]{({\gamma},{p})_{K}}(\Phi^{\prime\prime},\sigma^{\prime\prime})
for some
\gamma\in G(\mathbb{Q})
and
p\in P^{i}_{\Phi^{\prime}}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
, which induces a canonical isomorphism
\Delta^{i,\circ}_{\Phi^{\prime}}\backslash\mathrm{Sh}^{i}_{K_{\Phi^{\prime}}}(\sigma^{\prime})\cong\Delta^{i,\circ}_{\Phi^{\prime\prime}}\backslash\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi^{\prime\prime}}}^{i}(\sigma^{\prime\prime})
. So the first part is proved.

The second part follows from the first part and Proposition
1.34
.
∎

Proposition 1.43
.

Let
i=a
or
b
. Let
\mathrm{Sh}_{K}^{i,\Sigma}:=\mathrm{Sh}_{K}^{\Sigma}(G,X_{i})
be the toroidal compactification of
\mathrm{Sh}_{K}^{i}:=\mathrm{Sh}_{K}(G,X_{i})
associated with a
ZP
-invariant admissible cone decomposition
\Sigma
with the property
\mathcal{P}
as in Proposition
1.39
.
Then:

(1)

Let
\pi_{2}^{i}
be the projection
\pi_{2}^{i}:\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{Z}}\times\mathrm{Sh}_{K}^{i}\to\mathrm{Sh}_{K}^{i}
to the second factor. Then the pullback of
\Sigma
along
\pi_{2}^{i}
induces a cone decomposition
\pi^{i,*}_{2}(\Sigma)
, which we abusively denote by
\Sigma
. Then there is a canonical isomorphism
\mathrm{id}^{\Sigma}:(\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{Z}}\times\mathrm{Sh}_{K}^{i})^{\Sigma}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{Z}}\times\mathrm{Sh}_{K}^{i,\Sigma}
. So
[m^{\prime}]
extends to an isomorphism
[m^{\prime}]^{\Sigma}:\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{Z}}\times\mathrm{Sh}^{b,\Sigma}_{K}\to\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{Z}}\times\mathrm{Sh}^{a,\Sigma}_{K}
over
\widetilde{E}
.

(2)

Define

\mathrm{Z}_{[ZP^{i}(\Phi,\sigma)],K}:=\coprod_{[(\Phi^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime})]\in[ZP^{i}(\Phi,\sigma)]}\mathrm{Z}^{i}_{[(\Phi^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime})],K}.

Then the restriction of
m^{\prime}
to
ZP_{\Phi}^{b}
,
m^{\prime}:Z\times ZP^{b}_{\Phi}\to Z\times ZP^{a}_{\Phi}
, induces an isomorphism denoted by
[m^{\prime}]^{\Phi,\sigma}
,

\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{Z}}\times\coprod_{[(\Phi^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime})]\in[ZP^{b}(\Phi,\sigma)]}\Delta^{b,\circ}_{\Phi^{\prime},K}\backslash\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi^{\prime}}}^{b}(\sigma^{\prime})\xrightarrow{[m^{\prime}]^{\Phi,\sigma}}\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{Z}}\times\coprod_{[(\Phi^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime})]\in[ZP^{a}(\Phi,\sigma)]}\Delta^{a,\circ}_{\Phi^{\prime},K}\backslash\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi^{\prime}}}^{a}(\sigma^{\prime})

over
\widetilde{E}
. We also have a similar isomorphism for the unions of
\sigma^{\prime}
-closed strata.

(3)

The following diagram commutes:

(1.30)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{Z}})^{\widehat{}}_{[z]}\times(\mathrm{Sh}^{b,\Sigma}_{K})^{\widehat{}}_{\mathrm{Z}_{[ZP^{b}(\Phi,\sigma)],K}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{Z}})^{\widehat{}}_{[z]}\times\coprod\limits_{[(\Phi^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime})]\in[ZP^{b}(\Phi,\sigma)]}\Delta_{\Phi^{\prime},K}^{b,\circ}\backslash(\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi^{\prime}}}^{b}(\sigma^{\prime}))^{\widehat{}}_{\mathrm{Sh}^{b}_{K_{\Phi^{\prime}},\sigma^{\prime}}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{Z}})^{\widehat{}}_{[z]}\times(\mathrm{Sh}^{a,\Sigma}_{K})^{\widehat{}}_{\mathrm{Z}_{[ZP^{a}(\Phi,\sigma)],K}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{Z}})^{\widehat{}}_{[z]}\times\coprod\limits_{[(\Phi^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime})]\in[ZP^{a}(\Phi,\sigma)]}\Delta_{\Phi^{\prime},K}^{a,\circ}\backslash(\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi^{\prime}}}^{a}(\sigma^{\prime}))^{\widehat{}}_{\mathrm{Sh}^{a}_{K_{\Phi^{\prime}},\sigma^{\prime}}}}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[m^{\prime}]}^{\Sigma}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[m^{\prime}]}^{\Phi,\sigma}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}

Proof.

Again, we omit the subscripts of the level groups in the symbols of strata, cones and delta groups to simplify the notation.
We prove the first part with the same strategy as in Proposition
1.41
. For any cusp label
[\Phi^{\times}]
mapping to
[z]
and
[\Phi]
under
\pi_{1}^{\mathrm{min}}
and
\pi_{2}^{\mathrm{min}}
as in Proposition
1.41
, we have
\mathbf{P}_{\Phi^{\times}}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathbf{P}_{[z]}\times\mathbf{P}_{\Phi}=\mathbf{P}_{\Phi}
. For any cone
\sigma\in\Sigma(\Phi)
, we abusively denote by
\sigma
the cone induced by
\mathbf{P}_{\Phi^{\times}}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathbf{P}_{\Phi}
.
We have the following commutative diagram

(1.31)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi^{\times}}}(P_{\Phi^{\times}},D_{\Phi^{\times}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Z}_{[z]}\times\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi}}(P_{\Phi},D_{\Phi})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Sh}_{\overline{K}_{\Phi^{\times}}}(\overline{P}_{\Phi^{\times}},\overline{D}_{\Phi^{\times}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Z}_{[z]}\times\mathrm{Sh}_{\overline{K}_{\Phi}}(\overline{P}_{\Phi},\overline{D}_{\Phi}),}

where the horizontal morphisms are locally quasi-finite.
Then morphism
\mathbf{E}_{K_{\Phi^{\times}}}\to\mathbf{E}_{K_{\Phi}}
is also finite. So the morphism between twisted affine torus embeddings with respect to
\sigma
, i.e.,
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi^{\times}}}(P_{\Phi^{\times}},D_{\Phi^{\times}},\sigma)\to\mathrm{Z}_{[z]}\times\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi}}(P_{\Phi},D_{\Phi},\sigma)
, is locally quasi-finite.
So
\mathrm{Z}_{[(\Phi^{\times},\sigma)]}\to\mathrm{Z}_{[z]}\times\mathrm{Z}_{[(\Phi,\sigma)]}
is locally quasi-finite. Then
\mathrm{id}^{\Sigma}
is an isomorphism by Zariski’s main theorem, as it is a proper birational and quasi-finite morphism between normal schemes. Other statements in part one follow from
[
64
, 6.7(b) and 12.4(b)]
.

The morphism
m^{\prime}
induces an isomorphism

m^{\prime}:\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{Z}}\times\mathrm{Sh}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}^{b}}(ZP_{\Phi}^{b},ZP^{b}_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})D_{\Phi})\to\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{Z}}\times\mathrm{Sh}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}^{a}}(ZP^{a}_{\Phi},ZP^{a}_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})D_{\Phi})

by
[
64
, 3.5 (c)]
, whose quotient by
\widetilde{\Delta}^{ZP,\circ}_{\Phi}
on the second factor is
[m^{\prime}]^{\Phi,\sigma}
. So the second part is proved by Lemma
1.42
.
The third part follows from the first two parts, and
[
50
, Thm. 2.1.27 and 2.1.28]
. Note that the second paragraph also implies a similar isomorphism between closed strata.
∎

Define ??to be
\widetilde{E}_{K_{Z}}:=\widetilde{E}\cdot F_{K_{Z}}
. Then
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{Z}}
splits as a finite union of
\spec\widetilde{E}_{K_{Z}}
over
\widetilde{E}_{K_{Z}}
.

Corollary 1.44
.

With the conventions above, let
s:\spec\widetilde{E}_{K_{Z}}\to\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{Z}}
be any section of
(\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{Z}})_{\widetilde{E}_{K_{Z}}}
over
\widetilde{E}_{K_{Z}}
corresponding to an element
z\in Z(\mathbb{A}_{f})
. Denote by
\pi_{2}
the projection of a fiber product to the second factor. Denote by
[g]
the Hecke action of any element
g\in G(\mathbb{A}_{f})
. We have the following statements:

(1)

The compositions

(m^{\prime})^{\Phi}_{s}:=[z^{-1}]\circ\pi_{2}\circ(m^{\prime})\circ(s\times\mathrm{id}):\mathrm{Sh}_{\widetilde{K}^{b}_{\Phi}}(ZP^{b}_{\Phi},ZP^{b}_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})D_{\Phi})\to\mathrm{Sh}_{\widetilde{K}^{a}_{\Phi}}(ZP^{a}_{\Phi},ZP^{a}_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})D_{\Phi})

and

[m^{\prime}]^{\Phi}_{s}:=[z^{-1}]\circ\pi_{2}\circ[m^{\prime}]^{\Phi}\circ(s\times\mathrm{id}):\mathrm{Z}_{[ZP^{b}(\Phi)],K}\to\mathrm{Z}_{[ZP^{a}(\Phi)],K}

are isomorphisms over
\widetilde{E}_{K_{Z}}
. Also,
[m^{\prime}]^{\mathrm{min}}_{s}:=[z^{-1}]\circ\pi_{2}^{a}\circ[m^{\prime}]^{\mathrm{min}}\circ(s\times\mathrm{id}):\mathrm{Sh}^{b,\mathrm{min}}_{K}\to\mathrm{Sh}^{a,\mathrm{min}}_{K}
is an isomorphism over
\widetilde{E}_{K_{Z}}
extending
[m^{\prime}]_{s}:=[z^{-1}]\circ\pi_{2}^{a}\circ[m^{\prime}]\circ(s\times\mathrm{id})
.

(2)

Then the composition
[m^{\prime}]^{\Sigma}_{s}:=[z^{-1}]\circ\pi_{2}\circ[m^{\prime}]^{\Sigma}\circ(s\times\mathrm{id}):\mathrm{Sh}^{b,\Sigma}_{K}\to\mathrm{Sh}^{a,\Sigma}_{K}
is an isomorphism over
\widetilde{E}_{K_{Z}}
. Similarly, for any cusp label representative
\Phi
and any
\sigma\in\Sigma(\Phi)
, there is an isomorphism
[m^{\prime}]^{\Phi,\sigma}_{s}:=[z^{-1}]\circ\pi_{2}\circ[m^{\prime}]^{\Phi,\sigma}\circ\coprod(s\times\mathrm{id}):\coprod\limits_{[(\Phi^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime})]\in[ZP^{b}(\Phi,\sigma)]}\Delta^{b,\circ}_{\Phi^{\prime}}\backslash\mathrm{Sh}^{b}_{K_{\Phi^{\prime}}}(\sigma^{\prime})\to\coprod\limits_{[(\Phi^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime})]\in[ZP^{a}(\Phi,\sigma)]}\Delta^{a,\circ}_{\Phi^{\prime}}\backslash\mathrm{Sh}^{a}_{K_{\Phi^{\prime}}}(\sigma^{\prime})
over
\widetilde{E}_{K_{Z}}
, and it induces an isomorphism

\coprod\limits_{[(\Phi^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime})]\in[ZP^{b}(\Phi,\sigma)]}\Delta^{b,\circ}_{\Phi^{\prime},K}\backslash\mathrm{Sh}^{b}_{K_{\Phi^{\prime}},\sigma^{\prime}}\to\coprod\limits_{[(\Phi^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime})]\in[ZP^{a}(\Phi,\sigma)]}\Delta^{a,\circ}_{\Phi^{\prime},K}\backslash\mathrm{Sh}^{a}_{K_{\Phi^{\prime}},\sigma^{\prime}}

over
\widetilde{E}_{K_{Z}}
.

Moreover, the following diagram over
\widetilde{E}_{K_{Z}}
commutes:

(1.32)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\mathrm{Sh}^{b,\Sigma}_{K})^{\widehat{}}_{\mathrm{Z}_{[ZP^{b}(\Phi,\sigma)],K}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\coprod\limits_{[(\Phi^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime})]\in[ZP^{b}(\Phi,\sigma)]}\Delta_{\Phi^{\prime},K}^{b,\circ}\backslash(\mathrm{Sh}_{K}^{b}(\Phi^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime}))^{\widehat{}}_{\mathrm{Sh}^{b}_{K_{\Phi^{\prime}},\sigma^{\prime}}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\mathrm{Sh}^{a,\Sigma}_{K})^{\widehat{}}_{\mathrm{Z}_{[ZP^{a}(\Phi,\sigma)],K}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\coprod\limits_{[(\Phi^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime})]\in[ZP^{a}(\Phi,\sigma)]}\Delta_{\Phi^{\prime},K}^{a,\circ}\backslash(\mathrm{Sh}_{K}^{a}(\Phi^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime}))^{\widehat{}}_{\mathrm{Sh}^{a}_{K_{\Phi^{\prime}},\sigma^{\prime}}}}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[m^{\prime}]}^{\Sigma}_{s}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[m^{\prime}]}^{\Phi,\sigma}_{s}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}

(3)

All the isomorphisms
[m^{\prime}]_{s}
,
[m^{\prime}]^{\Phi}_{s}
,
[m^{\prime}]^{\mathrm{min}}_{s}
,
[m^{\prime}]^{\Sigma}_{s}
and
[m^{\prime}]^{\Phi,\sigma}_{s}
above are independent of the choice of
s
.

Proof.

By
[
64
, 12.4(b)]
and our construction, all of the morphisms above are defined over
\widetilde{E}_{K_{Z}}
. Then we can check all the statements above over field extensions.
Since all of
[m^{\prime}]^{?}
for
?=,\ \Phi,\ (\Phi,\sigma),\ \Sigma,\ \mathrm{min}
are isomorphisms according to Proposition
1.41
and Proposition
1.43
, and since
\pi_{2}
,
s\times\mathrm{id}
and
[z]
all induce isomorphisms on each geometrically connected component, we only need to count the number of geometrically connected components.
Over
\overline{\widetilde{E}}
, the morphism
[m^{\prime}]_{s}^{\Sigma}
(resp.
[m^{\prime}]_{s}^{\Phi,\sigma}
) induces isomorphism of connected components via
\pi_{0}(\mathrm{Sh}^{b,\Sigma}_{K})\to[z]\times\mathrm{Stab}_{G(\mathbb{Q})}(X^{+})\backslash G(\mathbb{A}_{f})/K\to[z]\times\mathrm{Stab}_{G(\mathbb{Q})}(X^{+})\backslash G(\mathbb{A}_{f})/K\to\pi_{0}(\mathrm{Sh}^{a,\Sigma}_{K})

(resp.
\pi_{0}(\coprod_{[(\Phi^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime})]\in[ZP^{b}(\Phi,\sigma)]}\mathrm{Sh}^{b}_{K_{\Phi^{\prime}}}(\sigma^{\prime}))\to

[z]\times\mathrm{Stab}_{Q(\mathbb{Q})}(D^{+}_{\Phi})\backslash ZP^{b}_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f})g_{\Phi}K/K\to

[z]\times\mathrm{Stab}_{Q(\mathbb{Q})}(D^{+}_{\Phi})\backslash ZP^{a}_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f})g_{\Phi}zK/K\to

\pi_{0}(\coprod_{[(\Phi^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime})]\in[ZP^{a}(\Phi,\sigma)]}\mathrm{Sh}^{a}_{K_{\Phi^{\prime}}}(\sigma^{\prime}))
) (see Lemma
1.19
). The morphism in the bracket is an isomorphism because of Lemma
1.40
and Lemma
1.42
. Other isomorphisms are checked in a similar way. So the first part is proved. The second part is also true since we can verify it over
\overline{\widetilde{E}}
, since we have (
1.30
) and since we can restrict it to geometrically connected components of
\mathrm{Sh}^{i,\Sigma}_{K}
, and open and closed subschemes of
\mathrm{Sh}^{b}_{K_{\Phi^{\prime}}}(\sigma^{\prime})
. Finally, we can check over complex points that
[m^{\prime}]_{s}
and
[m^{\prime}]_{s}^{\Phi}
are isomorphisms that are independent of the choice of
s
or
z
. Then the definitions of all the other morphisms are independent of the choice of
s
or
z
because of density.
∎

Convention 1.45
.

We denote
[m^{\prime}]_{s}
,
[m^{\prime}]^{\Phi}_{s}
,
[m^{\prime}]^{\mathrm{min}}_{s}
,
[m^{\prime}]^{\Sigma}_{s}
and
[m^{\prime}]^{\Phi,\sigma}_{s}
by
m
,
m^{\Phi}
,
m^{\mathrm{min}}
,
m^{\Sigma}
and
m^{\Phi,\sigma}
, respectively, since they do not depend on the choice of sections.

1.4.4.

We finally come to the main setup. Let
(G_{0},X_{0})
be a Hodge-type Shimura datum. Denote by
\iota:(G_{0},X_{0})\hookrightarrow(G^{\ddagger},X^{\ddagger})
an embedding into a Siegel Shimura datum. Let
(G_{2},X_{2})
be an abelian-type Shimura datum with a central isogeny
\pi^{\mathrm{der}}:G_{0}^{\mathrm{der}}\to G_{2}^{\mathrm{der}}
between the derived groups of
G_{0}
and
G_{2}
such that
\pi^{\mathrm{der}}
induces an isomorphism
(G_{0}^{\mathrm{ad}},X_{0}^{\mathrm{ad}})\cong(G_{2}^{\mathrm{ad}},X_{2}^{\mathrm{ad}})
.
If
G_{2,\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
is unramified, by
[
36
, Lem. 3.4.13]
, there is a
G_{0}
such that
G_{0,\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
is also unramified.

Construction 1.46
.

Let
C^{\mathrm{der}}
be the kernel of
\pi^{\mathrm{der}}
. Let
G^{\prime}_{0}:=G_{0}/C^{\mathrm{der}}
be the quotient of
G_{0}
by
C^{\mathrm{der}}
. Let
G^{*}
be the contracted product
G^{*}:=G_{2}\times^{G^{\mathrm{der}}_{2}}G^{\prime}_{0}
. The center
Z^{*}
of
G^{*}
can be embedded in some quasi-split
\mathbb{Q}
-torus
Z^{\prime}
by
[
66
, Prop. 2.1, p. 55]
. We define
G:=G^{*}\times^{Z^{*}}Z^{\prime}
.

By the construction in the last paragraph, there are natural homomorphisms
\pi^{a}:G_{2}\to G^{*}\to G
and
\pi^{b}:G_{0}\to G^{*}\to G
, where
\pi^{a}
is an embedding and
\pi^{b}
has finite kernel. Let
X_{a}
(resp.
X_{b}
) be the
G(\mathbb{R})
-orbit of one of the elements in
X_{2}
(resp.
X_{0}
).
Since
H^{1}(\mathbb{R},Z^{\prime})
is trivial by
[
66
, Lem. 2.4, p.73]
,
X_{a}\cong X_{b}\cong X^{\mathrm{ad}}
. In particular, the Shimura data
(G,X_{a})
and
(G,X_{b})
satisfy the assumptions in §
1.4.3
.

Fix an open compact subgroup
K_{2,p}
of
G_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
, and a neat open compact subgroup of
K_{2}^{p}
of
G_{2}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
. Choose an open compact
K_{p}\subset G(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
such that
K_{p}\cap G_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})=K_{2,p}
. Let
K_{0,p}
be an open compact subgroup of
G_{0}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
contained in the preimage of
K_{p}
under
\pi^{b}
. Let
K_{0}:=K_{0,p}K^{p}_{0}
(resp.
K:=K_{p}K^{p}
), where
K^{p}_{0}
(resp.
K^{p}
) is a neat open compact subgroup of
G_{0}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
(resp.
G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
).

By Proposition
1.35
, we can choose
K^{p}
sufficiently small such that
\pi^{a}
induces an open and closed embedding
\pi^{a}:\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{2}}(G_{2},X_{2})\hookrightarrow\mathrm{Sh}_{K}(G,X_{a})
and an isomorphism
\pi^{a}:\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi_{2}}}(P_{\Phi_{2}},D_{\Phi_{2}})\cong\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi}}(P_{\Phi},D_{\Phi}),
for any cusp label representative
\Phi_{2}
mapping to
\Phi
under
\pi^{a}
.

Let
\im G_{0}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
be the image of
G_{0}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
under
\pi^{b}
. Define an index set ??as follows:

I_{G/G_{0}}:=I_{G/G_{0},K}:=\mathrm{Stab}_{G(\mathbb{Q})}(X_{0})\im G_{0}(\mathbb{A}_{f})\backslash G(\mathbb{A}_{f})/K.

We omit the subscript
K
in the notation if it is clear in the context.
From now on, we fix a set of representatives
\{g_{\alpha}\}_{\alpha\in I_{G/G_{0}}}
of

; moreover, if

and

are all chosen to be unramified at

, Lem. 2.2.6]
, we can choose

in

.

For any
\alpha
, choose a neat open compact

of

contained in

, and choose an open compact subgroup

contained in

. Denote by

the morphism defined as in §

. For any

such that:

•

are open compact subgroups of

and
K_{p}^{{\ddagger},\alpha}\cap G_{0}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\supset K_{0,p}^{\alpha}
.

•

are neat open compact subgroups of

containing

.

•

\iota^{\alpha}:\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{0}^{\alpha}}(G_{0},X_{0})\to\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{{\ddagger},\alpha}}(G^{\ddagger},X^{\ddagger})
are finite morphisms.

Since
E_{a}\subset
??
:=E(G_{2},X_{2})
and
E_{b}\subset
??
:=E(G_{0},X_{0})
, we have
\widetilde{E}\subset

:=E_{0}\cdot E_{2}
.

Proposition 1.47
.

Let
\Sigma_{2}
be an admissible cone decomposition for
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{2}}(G_{2},X_{2})
. Then by Proposition
1.36
, there is an admissible
ZP
-invariant cone decomposition
\Sigma
for
\mathrm{Sh}_{K}(G,X_{a})
and
\mathrm{Sh}_{K}(G,X_{b})
such that
\pi^{a,*}(\Sigma)
refines
\Sigma_{2}
.

For each
g_{\alpha}
defined above, let
\Sigma_{0}^{\alpha}:=\pi^{b}(g_{\alpha})^{*}(\Sigma)
.
Then
\Sigma
can be chosen such that, for each
\alpha\in I_{G/G_{0}}
,
\Sigma_{0}^{\alpha}
is induced by an admissible cone decomposition
\Sigma^{{\ddagger},\alpha}
for
\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{{\ddagger},\alpha}}(G^{\ddagger},X^{\ddagger})
, and such that
\Sigma^{{\ddagger},\alpha}
is a refinement of a smooth or smooth and projective admissible cone decomposition for
\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{{\ddagger},\alpha}}(G^{\ddagger},X^{\ddagger})
. Moreover, we can further choose
\Sigma
to be smooth, projective, or to be both smooth and projective.

Proof.

First, we apply the argument of
[
47
, Prop. 4.10]
. For any
\alpha\in I_{G/G_{0}}
, we have an embedding
\iota^{\alpha}:\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{0}^{\alpha}}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{{\ddagger},\alpha}}
by construction. For any cusp label representative
\Phi_{0}^{\alpha}\in\mathcal{CLR}(G_{0},X_{0})
, the map
\iota^{\alpha}
sends
\Phi_{0}^{\alpha}
to a cusp label representative
\Phi^{{\ddagger},\alpha}\in\mathcal{CLR}(G^{\ddagger},X^{\ddagger})
. This assignment induces embeddings of cones
\iota^{\alpha}_{\mathbf{P}}:\mathbf{P}_{\Phi_{0}^{\alpha}}\hookrightarrow\mathbf{P}_{\Phi^{{\ddagger},\alpha}}
and
\mathbb{R}
-spaces
\iota^{\alpha}_{U}:U_{\Phi_{0}^{\alpha}}(\mathbb{R})(-1)\hookrightarrow U_{\Phi^{{\ddagger},\alpha}}(\mathbb{R})(-1)
. The maps
\iota^{\alpha}_{\mathbf{P}}
and
\iota^{\alpha}_{U}
are cut out by some hyperplane
H_{\Phi_{0}^{\alpha}}\subset U_{\Phi^{{\ddagger},\alpha}}(\mathbb{R})(-1)
.
By
loc. cit.
, there is a pair of admissible cone decompositions (which are not necessarily smooth or projective)
(\Sigma^{\alpha}_{0,\mathrm{scp}},\Sigma^{{\ddagger},\alpha}_{\mathrm{scp}})
, such that the two cone decompositions are
strictly compatible with each other
(see Definition
1.18
).
That is, for any
\Phi_{0}^{\alpha}
mapping to
\Phi^{{\ddagger},\alpha}
, and any
\sigma^{{\ddagger},\alpha}\in\Sigma^{{\ddagger},\alpha}_{\mathrm{scp}}(\Phi^{{\ddagger},\alpha})
such that
\sigma^{{\ddagger},\alpha,\circ}\cap H_{\Phi_{0}^{\alpha}}\neq\emptyset
, we have
\sigma^{{\ddagger},\alpha}\subset H_{\Phi_{0}^{\alpha}}
and
\sigma^{{\ddagger},\alpha}\in\Sigma^{\alpha}_{0,\mathrm{scp}}(\Phi_{0}^{\alpha})
. In other words, all hyperplanes
H_{\Phi_{0}^{\alpha}}
either intersect with the cone
\sigma^{{\ddagger},\alpha}
on a face of its boundaries, or contain the cone, and each
\sigma_{0}^{\alpha}\in\Sigma_{0,\mathrm{scp}}^{\alpha,+}(\Phi_{0}^{\alpha})
also maps exactly to a cone in
\Sigma_{\mathrm{scp}}^{{\ddagger},\alpha,+}(\Phi^{{\ddagger},\alpha})
. For any refinement
\Sigma_{0}^{\alpha}
of
\Sigma_{0,\mathrm{scp}}^{\alpha}
with property
\mathcal{P}
, since all the refinements are on the boundaries of each
\sigma^{{\ddagger},\alpha}
, or are on the whole
\sigma^{{\ddagger},\alpha}
. By
[
64
, Prop. 5.23 and Lem. 5.25]
and
[
30
, pp. 32-35]
, there is a refinement
\Sigma^{{\ddagger},\alpha}
of
\Sigma^{{\ddagger},\alpha}_{\mathrm{scp}}
such that
\Sigma_{0}^{\alpha}
and
\Sigma^{{\ddagger},\alpha}
are strictly compatible with each other.

Then it suffices to choose an admissible cone decomposition
\Sigma
for
\mathrm{Sh}_{K}(G,X_{b})
, such that the induced cone decompositions
\pi^{b}(g_{\alpha})^{*}(\Sigma)
are refinements of
\Sigma_{0,\mathrm{scp}}^{\alpha}
. Indeed, if we can do so, we can refine
\Sigma
further so that it satisfies the property
\mathcal{P}
and is
ZP
-invariant. Denote this refinement by
\Sigma^{\prime}
. Then the pullbacks
\pi^{b}(g_{\alpha})^{*}(\Sigma^{\prime})
are refinements of
\pi^{b}(g_{\alpha})^{*}(\Sigma)
and in particular, of
\Sigma_{0,\mathrm{scp}}^{\alpha}
. Then, by the last paragraph, there are refinements
\Sigma^{{\ddagger},\alpha,\prime}
of
\Sigma^{{\ddagger},\alpha}_{\mathrm{scp}}
, such that
\pi^{b}(g_{\alpha})^{*}(\Sigma^{\prime})
and
\Sigma^{{\ddagger},\alpha,\prime}
are strictly compatible with each other for all
\alpha
. In particular,
\pi^{b}(g_{\alpha})^{*}(\Sigma^{\prime})
are induced by
\Sigma^{{\ddagger},\alpha,\prime}
for all
\alpha
.

In fact, we can choose such a
\Sigma
. Since the maps
\mathrm{Cusp}_{K_{0}^{\alpha}}(G_{0},X_{0})\to\mathrm{Cusp}_{K}(G,X_{b})
induced by
\pi^{b}(g_{\alpha})
are maps between finite sets and with finite fibers and since
|I_{G/G_{0}}|
is finite, we only have finitely many prescribed cone decompositions for each
\mathbf{P}_{\Phi}
with
\Phi\in\mathcal{CLR}(G,X_{b})
. For any
\Phi_{0}^{\alpha}\in\mathcal{CLR}(G_{0},X_{0})
mapping to
\Phi\in\mathcal{CLR}(G,X_{b})
, the map
\pi^{b}(g_{\alpha})_{\mathbf{P}}:\mathbf{P}_{\Phi_{0}^{\alpha}}\to\mathbf{P}_{\Phi}
induced by
\pi^{b}(g_{\alpha})
is an isomorphism. We then choose a common refinement of the cone decompositions of all
[\Phi_{0}^{\alpha}]
in
\coprod_{\alpha\in I_{G/G_{0}}}\mathrm{Cusp}_{K_{0}^{\alpha}}(G_{0},X_{0})
mapping to
[\Phi]
; we can do this since there are only finitely many such cone decompositions for
[\Phi]
.
∎

Definition 1.48
.

As §
1.1.3
, define
\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}:=\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}^{i}:=ZP^{i}_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f})\cap g_{\Phi}Kg_{\Phi}^{-1}
,
\overline{\widetilde{K}}_{\Phi}:=\overline{\widetilde{K}}_{\Phi}^{i}:=ZP^{i}_{\Phi}/U_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f})\cap(g_{\Phi}Kg_{\Phi}^{-1}/g_{\Phi}Kg_{\Phi}^{-1}\cap U_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f}))
and
\widetilde{K}_{\Phi,h}:=\widetilde{K}_{\Phi,h}^{i}:=ZP^{i}_{\Phi}/W_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f})\cap(g_{\Phi}Kg_{\Phi}^{-1}/W_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f})\cap g_{\Phi}Kg_{\Phi}^{-1})
. Define
\mathrm{Sh}_{\widetilde{K}^{i}_{\Phi}}:=\mathrm{Sh}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}}(ZP^{i}_{\Phi},ZP^{i}_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})D_{\Phi})
,

\overline{\mathrm{Sh}}_{\widetilde{K}^{i}_{\Phi}}:=\mathrm{Sh}_{\overline{\widetilde{K}}_{\Phi}}(ZP^{i}_{\Phi}/U_{\Phi},ZP^{i}_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})\overline{D}_{\Phi})
, and

\mathrm{Sh}_{\widetilde{K}^{i}_{\Phi},h}:=\mathrm{Sh}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi,h}}(ZP^{i}_{\Phi}/W_{\Phi},ZP_{\Phi}^{i}(\mathbb{Q})D_{\Phi,h})
.

Let
??
be the algebraic torus whose group of cocharacters corresponds to the lattice
\bm{\Lambda}_{K_{\Phi}}\subset U_{\Phi}(\mathbb{R})(-1)
.
By
[
64
, 3.13]
and Lemma
1.14
, the variety
\mathrm{Sh}_{\widetilde{K}^{i}_{\Phi}}
is a
\mathbf{E}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}}
-torsor over
\overline{\mathrm{Sh}}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}^{i}}
, and
\overline{\mathrm{Sh}}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}^{i}}
is an abelian scheme torsor over
\mathrm{Sh}_{\widetilde{K}^{i}_{\Phi},h}
.

2.
One-motives and the theory of degeneration

The goal of this section is to review and explain Faltings-Chai’s theory of degenerating abelian schemes (see
[
21
]
and
[
44
]
). As a preparation for the next section, we present the theory in the language of
1
-motives and aim to relate the notions in different references (see, e.g.,
[
53
]
,
[
64
]
,
[
72
]
,
[
50
, §2.2]
,
[
21
]
,
[
44
]
and
[
43
]
).

We start with Siegel moduli and
1
-motives (see §
2.1
and §
2.2
).
We then review the theory of degeneration (see §
2.3
), and explain the moduli interpretations of boundary mixed Shimura varieties of integral models of Siegel Shimura varieties (see §
2.4
). In particular, we check that for Siegel Shimura varieties, definitions of cusp labels, boundary charts, and level structures of boundary charts given in various references are the same. In §
2.5
, we review the main theorems of toroidal compactifications of integral models of Siegel/PEL/Hodge-type Shimura varieties.
The results that will be used in subsequent sections will be proved throughout the course of the exposition.

Since we will only study Hodge-type and Siegel-type Shimura varieties in this section, the index set
I_{G/G_{0}}
in the previous section is not important here; all superscripts
\alpha
in the conventions of §
1.4.4
will be omitted.

2.1.
Siegel moduli

Recall that
G_{0}
is a connected reductive group over
\mathbb{Q}
and ??is a Hodge-type Shimura datum with an embedding
\iota:(G_{0},X_{0})\hookrightarrow
??
:=(\mathrm{GSp}(V,\psi),\mathbb{S}^{\pm})
. We denote
G^{\ddagger}:=\mathrm{GSp}(V,\psi)
and
X^{\ddagger}:=\mathbb{S}^{\pm}
. We denote by
\nu:=\nu_{G^{\ddagger}}:G^{\ddagger}\to\mathbb{G}_{m}
the similitude character of
G^{\ddagger}
. Let
K_{0}:=K_{0,p}K^{p}_{0}
(resp.
K^{\ddagger}:=K^{\ddagger}_{p}K^{{\ddagger},p}
) be a neat open compact subgroup of
G_{0}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
(resp.
G^{\ddagger}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
) such that
K_{0}\subset K^{\ddagger}\subset G^{\ddagger}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
, such that
K^{p}_{0}
(resp.
K^{{\ddagger},p}
) is a neat open compact subgroup of
G_{0}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
(resp.
G^{\ddagger}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
) and such that
K_{0,p}
(resp.
K^{\ddagger}_{p}
) is an open compact subgroup of
G_{0}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
(resp.
G^{\ddagger}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
).
By Zarhin’s trick, after replacing
V
with
V^{\oplus 8}
if necessary, we choose a
\mathbb{Z}
-lattice ??
\subset V
such that
V_{\mathbb{Z}}
is self-dual with respect to
\psi
, such that
K_{p}^{\ddagger}
is exactly the stabilizer of
V_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}
in
G^{\ddagger}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
, and such that
K^{\ddagger}
stabilizes
V_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}}
.

To avoid repeating similar definitions, let
\square
be
\emptyset
or
\{p\}
. If
\square=\emptyset
, then
K^{{\ddagger},\square}=K^{\ddagger}
and
K^{\ddagger}_{\square}
is empty. Denote by
\mathbf{M}_{K^{\ddagger},\mathbb{Z}_{(\square)}}^{\mathrm{isog}}
the moduli problem
\mathbf{M}_{K^{\ddagger},\mathbb{Z}_{(\square)}}^{\mathrm{isog}}:(Sch/\mathbb{Z}_{(\square)})^{\mathrm{op}}\to(Grpds)
defined as follows:

For any locally Noetherian and connected
\mathbb{Z}_{(\square)}
-scheme
S
,
\mathbf{M}_{K^{{\ddagger},\square}}^{\mathrm{isog}}(S)
is the groupoid of tuples
(\mathcal{A},\lambda,[\varepsilon^{\ddagger}]_{K^{{\ddagger},\square}})
, where

•

\mathcal{A}
is an abelian scheme over
S
,

•

\lambda
is a
\mathbb{Z}_{(\square)}^{\times}
-polarization (i.e.,
[N]\circ\lambda
is a (positive) polarization for some positive integer
N
such that
(N,\square)=1
), and

•

[\varepsilon^{\ddagger}]_{K^{{\ddagger},\square}}
is a
\pi_{1}(S,\overline{s})
-invariant
K^{{\ddagger},\square}
-orbit of
\mathbb{A}_{f}^{\square}
-equivariant isomorphisms
\varepsilon^{\ddagger}:V_{\mathbb{A}_{f}^{\square}}\xrightarrow{\sim}V^{p}\mathcal{A}_{\overline{s}}
at some (and therefore any) geometric point
\overline{s}
of
S
(this is equivalent to saying that
[\varepsilon^{\ddagger}]_{K^{{\ddagger},\square}}\in\Gamma(S,\isom(V_{\mathbb{A}_{f}^{\square}},V^{\square}\mathcal{A})/K^{{\ddagger},\square})
), such that,
\psi_{\mathbb{A}_{f}^{\square}}
is sent to an
\mathbb{A}_{f}^{\square,\times}
-multiple of the Weil pairing
\mathrm{e}^{\lambda_{\overline{s}}}
of
\lambda_{\overline{s}}
via each such
\varepsilon^{\ddagger}
in the orbit; we will omit the subscript
K^{{\ddagger},\square}
when it is clear in the context.

Any pair of such tuples
(\mathcal{A},\lambda,[\varepsilon^{\ddagger}])
and
(\mathcal{A}^{\prime},\lambda^{\prime},[\varepsilon^{{\ddagger},\prime}])
are equivalent in
\mathbf{M}^{\mathrm{isog}}_{K^{\ddagger}}(S)
, denoted as
(\mathcal{A},\lambda,[\varepsilon^{\ddagger}])\sim_{\mathrm{isog}}(\mathcal{A}^{\prime},\lambda^{\prime},[\varepsilon^{{\ddagger},\prime}])
, if there is a
\mathbb{Z}_{(\square)}^{\times}
-isogeny
f:\mathcal{A}\to\mathcal{A}^{\prime}
such that
rf^{\vee}\circ\lambda^{\prime}\circ f=\lambda
for some
\mathbb{Z}_{(\square),>0}^{\times}
and such that
V^{\square}f\circ\varepsilon^{\ddagger}=\varepsilon^{{\ddagger},\prime}
modulo the action of
K^{{\ddagger},\square}
.
Finally, for general
\mathbb{Z}_{(\square)}
-scheme
S
, as the pairs
(\mathcal{A},\lambda)
described above are finitely presented, there is a locally Noetherian
\mathbb{Z}_{(\square)}
-scheme
S_{1}
with a morphism
S\to S_{1}
over
\mathbb{Z}_{(\square)}
, such that
(\mathcal{A},\lambda)
is the pullback of a disjoint union of tuples described above over each connected component of
S_{1}
. The action of
\pi_{1}(S,\overline{s})
for any geometric point
\overline{s}\in S
on
V^{\square}\mathcal{A}
factors through the action of the étale fundamental group
\pi_{1}(S_{1},\overline{s}_{1})
at a geometric point
\overline{s}_{1}
of
S_{1}
by
[
19
, Arcata V. Cor. 3.3]
. Hence, the tuple
(\mathcal{A},\lambda,[\varepsilon^{\ddagger}])
is the pullback of a disjoint union of tuples described above over each connected component of
S_{1}
.

Remark 2.1
.

We will have equivalent interpretations of
\mathbf{M}^{\mathrm{isog}}_{K^{{\ddagger},\square}}
if doing the following changes: (1) We further require that the objects in
\mathbf{M}^{\mathrm{isog}}_{K^{{\ddagger},\square}}(S)
satisfy the (Kottwitz) determinantal condition (see
[
39
, §5]
and also
[
44
, 1.3.4]
). Since the
\mathcal{O}
in
loc. cit.
is
\mathbb{Z}
, the condition is empty for the Siegel-type case. (2) We can change the term “
\mathbb{Z}_{(\square)}^{\times}
-polarizations” to other terms such as “weak polarizations with respect to
\mathbb{Z}_{(\square)}^{\times}
” and also allow the
r
in the equivalence condition above to cover the whole
\mathbb{Z}_{(\square)}^{\times}
.

The moduli problem above, defined by
\mathbb{Z}_{(\square)}^{\times}
-isogeny classes of abelian schemes with additional structures, is equivalent to the one defined below using isomorphism classes of abelian schemes with additional structures.

Let
(V_{\mathbb{Z}},\psi_{\mathbb{Z}})
be a pair of
\mathbb{Z}
-lattice
V_{\mathbb{Z}}
with
\psi_{\mathbb{Z}}:V_{\mathbb{Z}}\times V_{\mathbb{Z}}\to\mathbb{Z}(1)
, the restriction of
\psi
to
V_{\mathbb{Z}}
which takes its values in
\mathbb{Z}(1)
. Recall that we require that
V_{\mathbb{Z}}
is self-dual with respect to
\psi
. Define the moduli problem
\mathbf{M}^{\mathrm{iso}}_{{(V_{\mathbb{Z}},\psi_{\mathbb{Z}})},K^{{\ddagger},\square}}:(Sch/\mathbb{Z}_{(\square)})^{\mathrm{op}}\to(Grpds)
of isomorphism classes of abelian schemes with additional structures as follows:

For any locally Noetherian and connected
\mathbb{Z}_{(\square)}
-scheme
S
,
\mathbf{M}_{(V_{\mathbb{Z}},\psi_{\mathbb{Z}}),K^{{\ddagger},\square}}^{\mathrm{iso}}(S)
is a groupoid whose objects consist of the tuples
(\mathcal{A},\lambda,[\varepsilon^{\ddagger}_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{\square}}]_{K^{{\ddagger},\square}})
, where

•

\mathcal{A}
is an abelian scheme over
S
,

•

\lambda
is a principal polarization, and

•

[\varepsilon^{\ddagger}_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{\square}}]_{K^{{\ddagger},\square}}
is a
\pi_{1}(S,\overline{s})
-invariant
K^{{\ddagger},\square}
-orbit of
\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{\square}
-equivariant isomorphisms
\varepsilon^{\ddagger}_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{\square}}:V_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{\square}}\xrightarrow{\sim}T^{\square}\mathcal{A}_{\overline{s}}
for some (and hence any) geometric point
\overline{s}
of
S
(this is equivalent to saying that
[\varepsilon^{\ddagger}_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{\square}}]_{K^{{\ddagger},\square}}\in\Gamma(S,\isom(V_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{\square}},T^{\square}\mathcal{A})/K^{{\ddagger},\square})
), such that,
\psi_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{\square}}
is sent to a
\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{\square,\times}
-multiple of the Weil pairing
\mathrm{e}^{\lambda_{\overline{s}}}
associated with the polarization
\lambda_{\overline{s}}
via each such
\varepsilon^{\ddagger}_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{\square}}
; we will omit the subscript
K^{{\ddagger},\square}
when it is clear in the context.

Any pair of such tuples
(\mathcal{A},\lambda,[\varepsilon^{\ddagger}_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{\square}}])
and
(\mathcal{A}^{\prime},\lambda^{\prime},[\varepsilon^{{\ddagger},\prime}_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{\square}}])
are equivalent in
\mathbf{M}^{\mathrm{iso}}_{(V_{\mathbb{Z}},\psi_{\mathbb{Z}}),K^{{\ddagger},\square}}(S)
, denoted as
(\mathcal{A},\lambda,[\varepsilon^{\ddagger}_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{\square}}])\sim_{\mathrm{iso}}(\mathcal{A}^{\prime},\lambda^{\prime},[\varepsilon^{{\ddagger},\prime}_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{\square}}])
, if there is an isomorphism
f:\mathcal{A}\to\mathcal{A}^{\prime}
such that
f^{\vee}\circ\lambda^{\prime}\circ f=\lambda
and such that
T^{\square}f\circ\varepsilon^{\ddagger}_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{\square}}=\varepsilon^{{\ddagger},\prime}_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{\square}}
modulo the action of
K^{{\ddagger},\square}
. For general
\mathbb{Z}_{(\square)}
-scheme
S
, as explained above the objects described above are finitely presented. For any
(\mathcal{A},\lambda,[\varepsilon^{\ddagger}_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{\square}}])
over
S
, there is a locally Noetherian
\mathbb{Z}_{(\square)}
-scheme
S_{1}
with a morphism
S\to S_{1}
over
\mathbb{Z}_{(\square)}
, such that
(\mathcal{A},\lambda,[\varepsilon^{\ddagger}_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{\square}}])
is the pullback of a disjoint union of tuples described above over each connected component of
S_{1}
.

Remark 2.2
.

Again, it is harmless to do the following modifications to the definition above: (1) Requiring (Kottwitz) determinantal conditions, which are in fact empty for our case. (2) Requiring that one of
\pm\lambda
to be a polarization, and that
f^{\vee}\circ\pm\lambda^{\prime}\circ f=\pm\lambda
in the definition of the equivalence relation
\sim_{\mathrm{iso}}
.

Remark 2.3
.

The definition of
\mathbf{M}^{\mathrm{iso}}_{(V_{\mathbb{Z}},\psi_{\mathbb{Z}}),K^{{\ddagger},\square}}
depends only on the base change of
(V_{\mathbb{Z}},\psi_{\mathbb{Z}})
to
\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{\square}
; that is, if
(V^{(1)}_{\mathbb{Z}},\psi^{(1)}_{\mathbb{Z}})
and
(V^{(2)}_{\mathbb{Z}},\psi^{(2)}_{\mathbb{Z}})
are symplectic
\mathbb{Z}
-lattices that are isomorphic over
\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{\square}
after base change, then the two moduli problems defined by the two symplectic lattices are identical, since only the base change
(V_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{\square}},\psi_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{\square}})
is involved in the definition above. We shall omit the subscript
(V_{\mathbb{Z}},\psi_{\mathbb{Z}})
if it is clear in the context.

In the definition above, the condition that
\lambda
is a principal polarization follows automatically from the self-duality of
V_{\mathbb{Z}}
with respect to
\psi
and the definition of
\varepsilon^{\ddagger}_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{\square}}
. In general, if we choose a
V_{\mathbb{Z}}
such that
V_{\mathbb{Z}}\subset V_{\mathbb{Z}}^{\vee}
, we may require that
\lambda
is a polarization with degree
d
, where
d:=|V_{\mathbb{Z}}^{\vee}/V_{\mathbb{Z}}|
.

For any object
(\mathcal{A},\lambda,[\varepsilon^{\ddagger}_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{\square}}])
in
\mathbf{M}^{\mathrm{iso}}_{K^{{\ddagger},\square}}(S)
, we can associate an object

(\mathcal{A},\lambda,[\varepsilon^{\ddagger}_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{\square}}\otimes\mathbb{Z}_{(\square)}])
in
\mathbf{M}^{\mathrm{isog}}_{K^{\ddagger}}(S)
by viewing
\lambda
as a
\mathbb{Z}_{(\square)}^{\times}
-polarization and by tensoring
\varepsilon^{\ddagger}_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{\square}}
with
\mathbb{Z}_{(\square)}
. Then the natural inclusion of equivalence relations from
\mathbf{M}^{\mathrm{iso}}_{K^{{\ddagger},\square}}(S)
to
\mathbf{M}^{\mathrm{isog}}_{K^{{\ddagger},\square}}(S)
induces a natural transformation
t:\mathbf{M}^{\mathrm{iso}}_{K^{{\ddagger},\square}}\to\mathbf{M}^{\mathrm{isog}}_{K^{{\ddagger},\square}}
, which is in fact an equivalence; see, e.g.,
[
44
, Prop. 1.4.3.4]
.

Let us recall the construction of the inverse
t^{-1}:\mathbf{M}^{\mathrm{isog}}_{K^{{\ddagger},\square}}(S)\to\mathbf{M}^{\mathrm{iso}}_{K^{{\ddagger},\square}}(S)
following
loc. cit.
:

Construction 2.4
.

First, we can assume that
S
is locally Noetherian and connected over
\mathbb{Z}_{(\square)}
.
Next, for any object
(\mathcal{A},\lambda,[\varepsilon^{\ddagger}])
in
\mathbf{M}^{\mathrm{isog}}_{K^{{\ddagger},\square}}(S)
and any representative
\varepsilon^{\ddagger}:V_{\mathbb{A}_{f}^{\square}}\to V^{\square}\mathcal{A}_{\overline{s}}
, the image
\varepsilon^{\ddagger}(V_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{\square}})
of
V_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{\square}}
in
V^{\square}\mathcal{A}_{\overline{s}}
is
\pi_{1}(S,\overline{s})
-invariant (since for any
\pi\in\pi_{1}(S,\overline{s})
,
\pi(\varepsilon^{\ddagger})=\varepsilon^{\ddagger}\circ k
for some
k\in K^{{\ddagger},\square}
and
K^{{\ddagger},\square}
stabilizes
V_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{\square}}
). Moreover,
\varepsilon^{\ddagger}(V_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{\square}})
contains a finite index sub-
\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{\square}
-lattice
{N}T^{\square}\mathcal{A}_{\overline{s}}
for some prime-to-
\square
integer
N>0
.

Then there is a homomorphism between abelian schemes
f_{1}:\mathcal{A}_{\overline{s}}\to\mathcal{A}^{\prime}_{\overline{s}}
over
\overline{s}
defined by taking the quotient of
\mathcal{A}_{\overline{s}}
by
K_{\overline{s}}:=\frac{1}{N}\varepsilon^{\ddagger}(V_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{\square}})/T^{\square}\mathcal{A}_{\overline{s}}\subset V^{\square}\mathcal{A}_{\overline{s}}/T^{\square}\mathcal{A}_{\overline{s}}=\mathcal{A}_{\overline{s},\mathrm{torsion}}
.

Since
K_{\overline{s}}
is
\pi_{1}(S,\overline{s})
-invariant, it extends to a finite étale commutative group scheme
K_{S}
over
S
contained in
\mathcal{A}[k]
. So
f_{1}
is the pullback to
\overline{s}
of some homomorphism
f:\mathcal{A}\to\mathcal{A}^{\prime}
defined by taking the quotient of
\mathcal{A}
by
K_{S}
. Hence, there is a
\mathbb{Z}_{(\square)}^{\times}
-isogeny defined by
f^{\prime}:\mathcal{A}\xleftarrow{[N]}\mathcal{A}\xrightarrow{f}\mathcal{A}^{\prime}
such that
V^{\square}f^{\prime}\circ\varepsilon^{\ddagger}(V_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{\square}})=T^{\square}\mathcal{A}^{\prime}
.
Now we have found another object
(\mathcal{A}^{\prime},\lambda^{\prime},[\varepsilon^{{\ddagger},\prime}])
in
\mathbf{M}^{\mathrm{isog}}_{K^{{\ddagger},\square}}(S)
equivalent to
(\mathcal{A},\lambda,[\varepsilon^{\ddagger}])
such that
f^{\prime,\vee}\circ\lambda^{\prime}\circ f^{\prime}=\lambda
and
V^{\square}f^{\prime}\circ\varepsilon^{\ddagger}=\varepsilon^{{\ddagger},\prime}
.
Finally, since
\mathbb{A}_{f}^{\square,\times}=\mathbb{Z}_{(\square)}^{\times}\cdot\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{\square,\times}
, and
K^{{\ddagger},\square}
stabilizes
V_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{\square}}
, we can choose a prime-to-
\square
polarization
\lambda^{\prime}_{0}
, which is a
\mathbb{Z}_{(\square),>0}^{\times}
-multiple of
\lambda^{\prime}
, such that
\psi_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{\square}}
is sent to a
\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{\square,\times}
-multiple of
\mathrm{e}^{\lambda_{\overline{s}}}
via any
\varepsilon^{{\ddagger},\prime}
in
[\varepsilon^{{\ddagger},\prime}]
. Hence, we have found an object
(\mathcal{A}^{\prime},\lambda^{\prime}_{0},[\varepsilon^{{\ddagger},\prime}])
in
\mathbf{M}^{\mathrm{iso}}_{K^{{\ddagger},\square}}(S)
.

When
K^{{\ddagger},\square}
is neat, the moduli problems
\mathbf{M}^{\mathrm{isog}}_{K^{{\ddagger},\square}}
and
\mathbf{M}^{\mathrm{iso}}_{(V_{\mathbb{Z}},\psi_{\mathbb{Z}}),K^{{\ddagger},\square}}
are representable by a smooth quasi-projective scheme
\mathcal{M}_{K^{{\ddagger},\square}}
over
\mathbb{Z}_{(\square)}
. When
\square
is empty,
\mathcal{M}_{K^{{\ddagger}}}
is (the canonical model of) Siegel Shimura variety ??
:=\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{\ddagger}}(G^{\ddagger},X^{\ddagger})
. When
\square=\{p\}
, we denote
\mathcal{M}_{K^{{\ddagger},\square}}
by ??
:=\mathcal{S}_{K^{\ddagger}_{p}K^{{\ddagger},p}}(G^{\ddagger},\mathbb{S}^{\pm})
, the integral model of the Siegel Shimura variety
\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{\ddagger}}
associated with the Shimura datum
(G^{\ddagger},\mathbb{S}^{\pm})
and the level subgroup
K^{\ddagger}:=K_{p}^{\ddagger}K^{{\ddagger},p}
, where
K^{\ddagger}_{p}
is hyperspecial. The inverse limit
\mathcal{S}_{K^{\ddagger}_{p}}:=\varprojlim_{K^{{\ddagger},p}}\mathcal{S}_{K^{\ddagger}_{p}K^{{\ddagger},p}}
is an integral canonical model of
\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{\ddagger}_{p}}:=\varprojlim_{K^{{\ddagger},p}}\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{\ddagger}_{p}K^{{\ddagger},p}}
that satisfies the extension property.

2.2.
1-motives

2.2.1.

We recall the definition of
1
-motives.

Definition 2.5

(
[
18
, (10.1.2) and (10.1.10)]
; see also
[
68
, Sec. 2]
and
[
50
, 1.1]
)
.

A
1-motive
??over a base scheme
S
is a tuple
\mathcal{Q}=(\underline{Y},\mathcal{G}^{\natural},T,A,\iota,c^{\vee})
, where:

(1)

\underline{Y}
is an étale locally constant finite free
\mathbb{Z}
-module over
S
,

(2)

\mathcal{G}^{\natural}
is a semi-abelian scheme over
S
,

(3)

T
is a torus over
S
and
A
is an abelian scheme over
S
, such that
\mathcal{G}^{\natural}
is an extension of
A
by
T
, i.e., there is an exact sequence
0\to T\xrightarrow{\ i\ }\mathcal{G}^{\natural}\xrightarrow{\ \pi\ }A\to 0
,

(4)

\iota:\underline{Y}\to\mathcal{G}^{\natural}
is a homomorphism from
\underline{Y}
to
\mathcal{G}^{\natural}
, whose composition with the natural quotient
\mathcal{G}^{\natural}\to A
is
c^{\vee}:\underline{Y}\to A
.

Let
\underline{X}:=\mathbf{X}^{*}(T)
be the sheaf of groups of characters of
T
. Then
3
is equivalent to

\mathit{\ref{c}}^{\prime}

There is a homomorphism
c:\underline{X}\to A^{\vee}
.

In fact, for any
\chi\in\underline{X}
, the
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-torsor produced by the pushout of
\mathcal{G}^{\natural}
under
-\chi:T\to\mathbb{G}_{m}
corresponds to an invertible sheaf
\mathcal{O}_{\chi}
in
A^{\vee}:=\underline{\Pic}^{0}_{e}(A/S)
; so
\mathcal{G}^{\natural}
is associated with a homomorphism
\underline{X}\to\underline{\Pic}_{e}^{0}(A/S)
, and this association is bijective (see
[
44
, Prop. 3.1.5.1]
). Note that there is an anti-equivalence

\underline{\Pic}_{e}^{0}(A/S)\xrightarrow{\ \sim\ }\underline{\Ext}^{1}_{S}(A,\mathbb{G}_{m}).

Remark 2.6
.

Let
X
be an
S
-scheme. We will use the same notation to denote a
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-extension of
X
and its corresponding invertible sheaf; we will make the precise meaning clear in the context.

Let
\mathcal{P}_{A}
be the Poincaré biextension of
A\times_{S}A^{\vee}
by
\mathbb{G}_{m}
.

Given
c
and
c^{\vee}
, the statement
4
is equivalent to

\mathit{\ref{iota}}^{\prime}

There is a trivialization of biextensions
\tau^{-1}:\mathbf{1}_{\underline{Y}\times\underline{X}}\to(c^{\vee}\times c)^{*}\mathcal{P}_{A}
over
\underline{Y}\times\underline{X}
; or equivalently, a trivialization of biextensions
\tau:\mathbf{1}_{\underline{Y}\times\underline{X}}\to(c^{\vee}\times c)^{*}\mathcal{P}_{A}^{\otimes-1}
, i.e., the inverse of
\tau^{-1}
.

In fact, after replacing
S
with an étale cover, for any
y\in Y
and
\chi\in X
,
(c^{\vee}(y)\times c(\chi))^{*}\mathcal{P}_{A}\cong\mathcal{O}_{\chi}|_{c^{\vee}(y)}
. Then the sections
\{\tau^{-1}(y,\chi):\mathcal{O}_{\chi}|_{c^{\vee}(y)}\to\mathcal{O}_{S}\}
determine and are determined by
\bigoplus_{\chi}\mathcal{O}_{\chi}|_{c^{\vee}(y)}\xrightarrow{\sum_{\chi}\tau^{-1}(y,\chi)}\mathcal{O}_{S}
, which is a section in
\mathcal{G}^{\natural}(S)
that projects to
c^{\vee}(y)\in A(S)
, since we can write
\mathcal{G}^{\natural}
as
\underline{\spec}_{A}(\bigoplus_{\chi\in X}\mathcal{O}_{\chi})
.

Symmetrically, given
c
and
c^{\vee}
,
\textit{\ref{iota}}^{\prime}
is equivalent to

\mathit{\ref{iota}}^{\prime\prime}

Let
\mathcal{G}^{\natural,\vee}
be the semi-abelian scheme over
S
determined by
c^{\vee}
under
[
44
, Prop. 3.1.5.1]
. Then there is a homomorphism
\iota^{\vee}:\underline{X}\to\mathcal{G}^{\natural,\vee}
.

Note that the semi-abelian scheme
\mathcal{G}^{\natural,\vee}
satisfies an exact sequence
0\to T^{\vee}\xrightarrow{\ i^{\vee}\ }\mathcal{G}^{\natural,\vee}\xrightarrow{\ \pi^{\vee}\ }A^{\vee}\to 0
, which makes it an extension of
A^{\vee}
by
T^{\vee}:=\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}(\underline{Y},\mathbb{G}_{m})
.

Hence, a 1-motive
\mathcal{Q}
determines and is determined by its
Cartier dual

\mathcal{Q}^{\vee}:=(\underline{X},\mathcal{G}^{\natural,\vee},T^{\vee},A^{\vee},\iota^{\vee},c).

If there is more than one
1
-motive we are working with, we will use
?_{\mathcal{Q}}^{\sharp}
, for
?=\underline{Y},\underline{X},\mathcal{G}^{\natural},T,A,c,\iota,\tau
and for
\sharp=\emptyset,\vee
, to refer to the corresponding objects determined by
\mathcal{Q}
; if the
1
-motive we are referring to is clear in the context, we omit the subscript
\mathcal{Q}
.

Remark 2.7
.

Note that if
S
is the spectrum of a Noetherian complete local ring, or if
S
is locally Noetherian and normal, then every torus over
S
is isotrivial. See
[
3
, EX. X, 3 and 5]
; see also
[
44
, Rmk. 3.2.5.6]
. In these cases, we can require
\underline{Y}
to be trivialized by a finite étale cover in the definition of a
1
-motive
\mathcal{Q}
, and
\underline{X}=\underline{Y}_{\mathcal{Q}^{\vee}}
is also trivialized by a finite étale cover.

A
homomorphism
between 1-motives
f:\mathcal{Q}_{1}\to\mathcal{Q}_{2}
is a pair
f=(f^{et},f^{sab})\in\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}(\underline{Y}_{\mathcal{Q}_{1}},\underline{Y}_{\mathcal{Q}_{2}})\times\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}(\mathcal{G}^{\natural}_{\mathcal{Q}_{1}},\mathcal{G}^{\natural}_{\mathcal{Q}_{2}})
, such that
\iota_{\mathcal{Q}_{2}}\circ f^{et}=f^{sab}\circ\iota_{\mathcal{Q}_{1}}
. The morphism
f^{sab}
determines and is determined by a pair
(f^{tr},f^{ab})\in\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}(T_{\mathcal{Q}_{1}},T_{\mathcal{Q}_{2}})\times\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}(A_{\mathcal{Q}_{1}},A_{\mathcal{Q}_{2}})
. Moreover, define the
Cartier dual
f^{\vee}:\mathcal{Q}_{2}^{\vee}\to\mathcal{Q}_{1}^{\vee}
of
f
to be
f^{\vee}:=(f^{tr,\vee},(f^{et,\vee},f^{ab,\vee}))
.

Definition 2.8

(
[
2
, Sec. 1.3]
; see also
[
18
, 10.1.5]
)
.

For any positive integer
n
, define
n
-torsion subgroup of
\mathcal{Q}
to be the quotient sheaf

\mathcal{Q}[n]:=\frac{\ker((-\iota+[n]):\underline{Y}\times_{S}\mathcal{G}^{\natural}\to\mathcal{G}^{\natural})}{\im(([n],\iota):\underline{Y}\to\underline{Y}\times_{S}\mathcal{G}^{\natural})}.

Note that this sheaf
\mathcal{Q}[n]
is indeed representable by a finite flat commutative group over
S
: To see this, note that there is a canonical exact sequence of fppf sheaves:

(2.1)

0\to\mathcal{G}^{\natural}[n]\to\mathcal{Q}[n]\to\underline{Y}/n\underline{Y}\to 0.

Also, there is a canonical exact sequence of commutative groups over
S
:

(2.2)

0\to T[n]\to\mathcal{G}^{\natural}[n]\to A[n]\to 0.

Since finiteness and flatness are fppf-local on the base (see
[
71
,
Lem. 02LA
]
and
[
71
,
Lem. 02L2
]
),
\mathcal{G}^{\natural}[n]
is finite flat over
A[n]
. So
\mathcal{G}^{\natural}[n]
is finite flat over
S
.
In (
2.1
), the first and the last (non-zero) terms are representable. Since
\mathcal{G}^{\natural}[n]
is finite over
S
,
\mathcal{Q}[n]
is representable by fppf descent of affine morphisms; see

[
62
, Prop. 17.4]
, and also
[
71
,
Lem. 0247
]
; this also implies that
\mathcal{Q}[n]
is finite flat over
S
by
[
71
,
Lem. 02LA
]
and
[
71
,
Lem. 02L2
]
again.

Remark 2.9
.

Note that
one might also replace
(-\iota+[n])
with
(\iota+[n])
and replace
([n],\iota)
with
([n],-\iota)
to have a different sign convention for
\mathcal{Q}[n]
. Here we are following the conventions of
[
18
, 10.1.5]
so that we can define Weil pairings of
1
-motives the same as
[
18
, 10.2.5]
; moreover, it makes it easy to interpret splittings of the weight filtration
W
on
\mathcal{Q}[n]
as liftings
(c_{n},c^{\vee}_{n},\iota_{n})
(see Proposition
2.39
, part
3
).

Remark 2.10
.

Continuing the previous remark, as in
[
18
, 10.1.5]
,
\mathcal{Q}
is represented by a complex
[\underline{Y}\to\mathcal{G}^{\natural}]\in D^{b}_{fppf}(S)
, where
\underline{Y}
is in degree
-1
and
\mathcal{G}^{\natural}
is in degree
0
and where
D^{b}_{fppf}(S)
is the derived category of bounded complexes of fppf-sheaves of abelian groups over
S
. Moreover, by
[
68
, Prop. 2.3.1]
,
\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}_{1-\text{mot}}(\mathcal{Q}_{1},\mathcal{Q}_{2})=\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}_{D^{b}_{fppf}(S)}(\mathcal{Q}_{1},\mathcal{Q}_{2})
. We denote by
\{k\}
the
k
-shifted complex (since square brackets are occupied by the notation of
n
-torsions). So the multiplication of
\mathcal{Q}\{-1\}
by
n
is represented by the following morphism between complexes

(2.3)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[\underline{Y}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{G}^{\natural}]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[\underline{Y}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{G}^{\natural}]};}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces-\iota}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces n}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces n}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces-\iota}

the mapping cone of the above morphism is represented by

\mathcal{C}_{n}(\mathcal{Q}):=[\underline{Y}\xrightarrow{(n,\iota)}\underline{Y}\times_{S}\mathcal{G}^{\natural}\xrightarrow{-\iota+n}\mathcal{G}^{\natural}],

whose degrees are concentrated in
-1,0
and
1
. Then
\mathcal{Q}[n]=H^{0}(\mathcal{C}_{n}(\mathcal{Q}))\cong\mathcal{C}_{n}(\mathcal{Q}).

If we do not shift
\mathcal{Q}
by
-1
, we can also formulate
\mathcal{Q}[n]
as
H^{-1}
of
[\mathcal{Q}\xrightarrow{n}\mathcal{Q}]
(see, e.g.,
[
68
, 3.1]
and
[
50
, §1.1.2]
).

Lemma 2.11
.

Let
f:\mathcal{Q}_{1}\to\mathcal{Q}_{2}
be a homomorphism between
1
-motives over
S
. Then
f
naturally induces a homomorphism, which we abusively denote by
f:\mathcal{Q}_{1}[n]\to\mathcal{Q}_{2}[n]
, between
n
-torsion subgroups.

Proof.

This is just the consequence of the following commutative diagram:

(2.4)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}_{n}(\mathcal{Q}_{1}):}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{Y}_{\mathcal{Q}_{1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\underline{Y}_{\mathcal{Q}_{1}}\times_{S}\mathcal{G}^{\natural}_{\mathcal{Q}_{1}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{G}^{\natural}_{\mathcal{Q}_{1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}_{n}(\mathcal{Q}_{2}):}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{Y}_{\mathcal{Q}_{2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{Y}_{\mathcal{Q}_{2}}\times_{S}\mathcal{G}^{\natural}_{\mathcal{Q}_{2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{G}^{\natural}_{\mathcal{Q}_{2}}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(n,\iota_{\mathcal{Q}_{1}})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{et}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces-\iota_{\mathcal{Q}_{1}}+n}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(f^{et},f^{sab})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{sab}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces({n,\iota_{\mathcal{Q}_{2}}})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces-\iota_{\mathcal{Q}_{2}}+n}

∎

In general,

Definition 2.12
.

A homomorphism
f:\mathcal{Q}_{1}\to\mathcal{Q}_{2}
between
1
-motives is called an
isogeny
if
f^{et}
is injective and has finite cokernel, and if
f^{sab}
is an isogeny, that is,
f^{sab}
is surjective and finite.

Define the
kernel
of
f
to be the quotient sheaf

\ker f:=\frac{\ker((-\iota_{\mathcal{Q}_{2}}+f^{sab}):\underline{Y}_{\mathcal{Q}_{2}}\times_{S}\mathcal{G}_{\mathcal{Q}_{1}}^{\natural}\to\mathcal{G}_{\mathcal{Q}_{2}}^{\natural})}{\im((f^{et},\iota_{\mathcal{Q}_{1}}):\underline{Y}_{\mathcal{Q}_{1}}\to\underline{Y}_{\mathcal{Q}_{2}}\times_{S}\mathcal{G}^{\natural}_{\mathcal{Q}_{1}})}.

Equivalently,
\ker f:=H^{0}(\mathcal{C}_{f}(\mathcal{Q}_{1},\mathcal{Q}_{2}))\cong\mathcal{C}_{f}(\mathcal{Q}_{1},\mathcal{Q}_{2})
, where

\mathcal{C}_{f}(\mathcal{Q}_{1},\mathcal{Q}_{2}):=[\underline{Y}_{\mathcal{Q}_{1}}\xrightarrow{(f^{et},\iota_{\mathcal{Q}_{1}})}\underline{Y}_{\mathcal{Q}_{2}}\times_{S}\mathcal{G}_{\mathcal{Q}_{1}}^{\natural}\xrightarrow{-\iota_{\mathcal{Q}_{2}}+f^{sab}}\mathcal{G}_{\mathcal{Q}_{2}}^{\natural}],

the mapping cone of
f\{-1\}
,
whose degrees are concentrated in
-1,0
and
1
.

See more definitions about isogenies in Appendix
B
.

As (
2.1
), there is a canonical exact sequence:

(2.5)

0\to\ker f^{sab}\to\ker f\to\operatorname{coker}f^{et}\to 0.

By
[
62
, Prop. 17.4]
,
[
71
,
Lem. 02LA
]
,
[
71
,
Lem. 02L2
]
and
[
71
,
Lem. 0247
]
again,
\ker f
is representable by a finite flat commutative group over
S
.

Lemma 2.13
.

Let
f:\mathcal{Q}_{1}\to\mathcal{Q}_{2}
be an isogeny between
1
-motives over
S
. Then there is an integer
N
and an isogeny
h:\mathcal{Q}_{2}\to\mathcal{Q}_{1}
, such that,
h\circ f=[N]_{\mathcal{Q}_{1}}
and
f\circ h=[N]_{\mathcal{Q}_{2}}
. In particular,
\ker f\subset\mathcal{Q}_{1}[N]
and
\ker h\subset\mathcal{Q}_{2}[N]
.

Proof.

There is an integer
N_{1}
and an injective homomorphism
g^{et}:\underline{Y}_{\mathcal{Q}_{1}}\to\underline{Y}_{\mathcal{Q}_{2}}
with finite cokernel such that
g^{et}\circ f^{et}=[N_{1}]_{\underline{Y}_{\mathcal{Q}_{1}}}
and
f^{et}\circ g^{et}=[N_{1}]_{\underline{Y}_{\mathcal{Q}_{2}}}
; moreover, there is an integer
N_{2}
and an isogeny
g^{sab}:\mathcal{G}^{\natural}_{\mathcal{Q}_{1}}\to\mathcal{G}^{\natural}_{\mathcal{Q}_{2}}
such that
g^{sab}\circ f^{sab}=[N_{2}]_{\mathcal{G}^{\natural}_{\mathcal{Q}_{1}}}
and
f^{sab}\circ g^{sab}=[N_{2}]_{\mathcal{G}^{\natural}_{\mathcal{Q}_{2}}}
. Take
N:=N_{1}N_{2}
. Then
h:=([N_{2}]\circ g^{et},[N_{1}]\circ g^{sab})
satisfies the first statement: To see this, we draw the following commutative diagram

(2.6)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{Y}_{\mathcal{Q}_{1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{G}^{\natural}_{\mathcal{Q}_{1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{Y}_{\mathcal{Q}_{2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{G}^{\natural}_{\mathcal{Q}_{2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{Y}_{\mathcal{Q}_{1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{G}^{\natural}_{\mathcal{Q}_{1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{Y}_{\mathcal{Q}_{2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{G}^{\natural}_{\mathcal{Q}_{2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{Y}_{\mathcal{Q}_{1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{G}^{\natural}_{\mathcal{Q}_{1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{Y}_{\mathcal{Q}_{2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{G}^{\natural}_{\mathcal{Q}_{2}}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota_{\mathcal{Q}_{1}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces[N_{1}]}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces[N_{1}]}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota_{\mathcal{Q}_{2}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces[N_{1}]}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g^{et}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces[N_{1}]}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota_{\mathcal{Q}_{1}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces[N_{2}]}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces[N_{2}]}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota_{\mathcal{Q}_{2}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces[N_{2}]}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g^{sab}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces[N_{2}]}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota_{\mathcal{Q}_{1}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}

In the diagram above, all triangles, parallelograms and trapezoids containing at most one dashed arrow in their edges are commutative.
By diagram-chasing, we find
[N_{1}]\circ g^{sab}\circ\iota_{\mathcal{Q}_{2}}=\iota_{\mathcal{Q}_{1}}\circ[N_{2}]\circ g^{et}
.

For the second statement, we only need to show
\ker f\subset\mathcal{Q}_{1}[N]
, since the other one can be proved symmetrically. We have the following commutative diagram

(2.7)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}_{f}(\mathcal{Q}_{1},\mathcal{Q}_{2}):}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{Y}_{\mathcal{Q}_{1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\underline{Y}_{\mathcal{Q}_{2}}\times_{S}\mathcal{G}^{\natural}_{\mathcal{Q}_{1}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{G}^{\natural}_{\mathcal{Q}_{2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}_{N}(\mathcal{Q}_{1}):}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{Y}_{\mathcal{Q}_{1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{Y}_{\mathcal{Q}_{1}}\times_{S}\mathcal{G}^{\natural}_{\mathcal{Q}_{1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{G}^{\natural}_{\mathcal{Q}_{1}}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(f^{et},\iota_{\mathcal{Q}_{1}})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces-\iota_{\mathcal{Q}_{2}}+f^{sab}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(h^{et},\mathrm{Id})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h^{sab}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces({[N],\iota_{\mathcal{Q}_{1}}})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces-\iota_{\mathcal{Q}_{1}}+{[N]}}

Since
\ker(-\iota_{\mathcal{Q}_{2}}+f^{sab})\subset\ker(-\iota_{\mathcal{Q}_{1}}+[N])
and
\im(h^{et}\circ f^{et},\iota_{\mathcal{Q}_{1}})=\im([N],\iota_{\mathcal{Q}_{1}})
, we have the second statement.
∎

Conversely,

Lemma 2.14
.

Let
\mathcal{Q}_{1}
and
N
be defined as in Lemma
2.13
. Let
K
be a finite, flat, closed
S
-subgroup of
\mathcal{Q}_{1}[N]
.

Then there is a
1
-motive
\mathcal{Q}_{2}
and an isogeny
f:\mathcal{Q}_{1}\to\mathcal{Q}_{2}
, such that,
\ker f=K
.

Proof.

There is a canonical quotient map from
\mathcal{Q}_{1}[N]
to
\underline{Y}_{\mathcal{Q}_{1}}/N\underline{Y}_{\mathcal{Q}_{1}}
, whose kernel is
\mathcal{G}^{\natural}_{\mathcal{Q}_{1}}[N]
. Then
K
fits into a canonical exact sequence of fppf sheaves

0\to K_{G}\longrightarrow K\longrightarrow K_{Y}\longrightarrow 0.

In the sequence above,
K_{G}
is the kernel of
K\subset\mathcal{Q}_{1}[N]\to\underline{Y}_{\mathcal{Q}_{1}}/N\underline{Y}_{\mathcal{Q}_{1}}
, and
K_{Y}:=K/K_{G}
as an fppf sheaf; in fact,
K_{Y}=K/K_{G}
is representable by a scheme since
K_{G}
is finite. Since both
K
and
K_{G}
are flat,
K_{Y}
is finite and flat in
\underline{Y}_{\mathcal{Q}_{1}}/N\underline{Y}_{\mathcal{Q}_{1}}
, and therefore is locally constant.

Let
\mathcal{G}^{\natural}_{\mathcal{Q}_{2}}:=\mathcal{G}^{\natural}_{\mathcal{Q}_{1}}/K_{G}
and
\underline{Y}_{\mathcal{Q}_{2}}
be the inverse image of
K_{Y}\subset\underline{Y}_{\mathcal{Q}_{1}}/N\underline{Y}_{\mathcal{Q}_{1}}\cong\frac{1}{N}\underline{Y}_{\mathcal{Q}_{1}}/\underline{Y}_{\mathcal{Q}_{1}}
in
\frac{1}{N}\underline{Y}_{\mathcal{Q}_{1}}
.
Let
K_{T}
be the kernel of
K_{G}\subset\mathcal{G}^{\natural}_{\mathcal{Q}_{1}}[N]\to A_{\mathcal{Q}_{1}}[N]
and let
K_{A}:=K_{G}/K_{T}
, which is representable by a finite flat group scheme since
K_{T}
is finite and flat. There is an exact sequence
0\to K_{T}\to K_{G}\to K_{A}\to 0
.

Since
\mathcal{G}^{\natural}_{\mathcal{Q}_{1}}
is an extension of an abelian scheme
A_{\mathcal{Q}_{1}}
by a torus
T_{\mathcal{Q}_{1}}
, and by a theorem of Deligne and Raynaud
[
21
, Thm. 1.9 (b)]
, each orbit of
K_{G}
is locally on the base contained in an affine open subset of
\mathcal{G}^{\natural}_{\mathcal{Q}_{1}}
. Then
\mathcal{G}^{\natural}_{\mathcal{Q}_{2}}
is representable by a scheme. Then
\mathcal{G}^{\natural}_{\mathcal{Q}_{2}}
is an extension of an abelian scheme
A_{\mathcal{Q}_{2}}:=A_{\mathcal{Q}_{1}}/K_{A}
by a torus
T_{\mathcal{Q}_{2}}:=T_{\mathcal{Q}_{1}}/K_{T}
, and
\underline{Y}_{\mathcal{Q}_{2}}
is flat and locally constant.

Let
f^{et}:\underline{Y}_{\mathcal{Q}_{1}}\to\underline{Y}_{\mathcal{Q}_{2}}
be the natural inclusion; let
f^{sab}:\mathcal{G}^{\natural}_{\mathcal{Q}_{1}}\to\mathcal{G}^{\natural}_{\mathcal{Q}_{2}}
be the natural quotient.

Finally, let us define
\iota_{\mathcal{Q}_{2}}
such that
f^{sab}\circ\iota_{\mathcal{Q}_{1}}=\iota_{\mathcal{Q}_{2}}\circ f^{et}
. Fix any
S^{\prime}
over
S
and any
y\in\underline{Y}_{\mathcal{Q}_{2}}(S^{\prime})
.
Since
\underline{Y}_{\mathcal{Q}_{2}}
is defined to be the fiber product of
K_{Y}
and
\frac{1}{N}\underline{Y}_{\mathcal{Q}_{1}}
over
\frac{1}{N}\underline{Y}_{\mathcal{Q}_{1}}/\underline{Y}_{\mathcal{Q}_{1}}
, we see
y
projects to some
k_{y}\in K_{Y}(S^{\prime})
. We then lift
k_{y}
to
k\in K(S^{\prime})
and then to
\widetilde{k}=(\widetilde{y},\widetilde{g})\in\underline{Y}_{\mathcal{Q}_{1}}\times_{S}\mathcal{G}_{\mathcal{Q}_{1}}^{\natural}(S^{\prime})
up to replacing
S^{\prime}
with an fppf cover. Since the projection of
\widetilde{y}
from
\underline{Y}_{\mathcal{Q}_{1}}(S^{\prime})
to
\underline{Y}_{\mathcal{Q}_{1}}/N\underline{Y}_{\mathcal{Q}_{1}}(S^{\prime})\xrightarrow{\frac{1}{N}}\frac{1}{N}\underline{Y}_{\mathcal{Q}_{1}}/\underline{Y}_{\mathcal{Q}_{1}}(S^{\prime})
coincides with the projection of
y
to
\frac{1}{N}\underline{Y}_{\mathcal{Q}_{1}}/\underline{Y}_{\mathcal{Q}_{1}}(S^{\prime})
. We have
Ny-\widetilde{y}=Ny_{0}
for
y_{0}\in\underline{Y}_{\mathcal{Q}_{1}}(S^{\prime})
. By adjusting
(Ny_{0},\iota_{\mathcal{Q}_{1}}(y_{0}))
, we have a lifting
(Ny,\widetilde{g}^{\prime})\in\underline{Y}_{\mathcal{Q}_{1}}\times_{S}\mathcal{G}_{1}^{\natural}(S^{\prime})
. We define
\iota_{\mathcal{Q}_{2}}(y)=\overline{\widetilde{g}}^{\prime}
, the projection of
\widetilde{g}^{\prime}
to
\mathcal{G}_{\mathcal{Q}_{2}}^{\natural}(S^{\prime}).

This definition is independent of the choice of the lifting from
K_{Y}
to
K
, since any two liftings differ by an element in
K_{G}
. Moreover, this definition is independent of the second step of lifting from
K
to
\underline{Y}_{\mathcal{Q}_{1}}\times_{S}\mathcal{G}_{\mathcal{Q}_{1}}^{\natural}
, since we will always adjust it by some
(Ny_{0},\iota_{\mathcal{Q}_{1}}(y_{0}))
so that the first component is
Ny
. Hence, this map is well defined. It is compatible with
\iota_{\mathcal{Q}_{1}}
since if
y\in\underline{Y}_{\mathcal{Q}_{1}}(S^{\prime})
, the lifting we choose will be
(Ny,\iota_{\mathcal{Q}_{1}}(y))
.
∎

Moreover, we can define the
p
-adic Tate module by the
p
-adic sheaf
T_{p}\mathcal{Q}:=\varprojlim_{n\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}}\mathcal{Q}[p^{n}]
and denote the prime-to-
p
Tate module by the
\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{p}
-sheaf
T^{p}\mathcal{Q}:=\varprojlim_{n\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}\text{ and }p\nmid n}\mathcal{Q}[n]
. Define
\widehat{T}\mathcal{Q}:=\varprojlim_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\mathcal{Q}[n]
. Define the corresponding rational Tate modules by
V_{p}\mathcal{Q}:=T_{p}\mathcal{Q}\otimes\mathbb{Q}
,
V^{p}\mathcal{Q}:=T^{p}\mathcal{Q}\otimes\mathbb{Q}
and
\widehat{V}\mathcal{Q}:=\widehat{T}\mathcal{Q}\otimes\mathbb{Q}
, respectively.

The
weight filtration
W_{\bullet}
on
\mathcal{Q}
is defined by
W_{-3}\mathcal{Q}=0
,
W_{-2}\mathcal{Q}=T
,
W_{-1}\mathcal{Q}=\mathcal{G}^{\natural}
and
W_{0}\mathcal{Q}=\mathcal{Q}
; so
\mathrm{Gr}_{-2}^{W}=T
,
\mathrm{Gr}_{-1}^{W}=A
and
\gr_{0}^{W}=\underline{Y}
. This weight filtration canonically induces weight filtrations on
?\mathcal{Q}
by setting
W_{i}?\mathcal{Q}:=?W_{i}\mathcal{Q}
, for
?=T^{p}
,
T_{p}
,
V^{p}
,
V_{p}
,
\widehat{T}
and
\widehat{V}
, and for any
i\in\mathbb{Z}
; we denote their corresponding graded pieces by
\gr_{i}^{W_{?\mathcal{Q}}}
. For
n
-torsion subgroups, denote the weight filtration and its graded pieces by
W_{i}\mathcal{Q}[n]:=(W_{i}\mathcal{Q})[n]
and
\gr_{i}^{W_{\mathcal{Q}[n]}}
.

Lemma 2.15
.

Let
\kappa
be a field. Let
l\neq\chara\kappa
be a prime number. Let
\mathcal{Q}_{1}
and
\mathcal{Q}_{2}
be two
1
-motives over
S=\spec\kappa
. Then there is an injective map

(2.8)

T_{l}:\mathrm{Hom}(\mathcal{Q}_{1},\mathcal{Q}_{2})\hookrightarrow\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathbb{Z}_{l}}(T_{l}\mathcal{Q}_{1},T_{l}\mathcal{Q}_{2}).

Proof.

Let
f:\mathcal{Q}_{1}\to\mathcal{Q}_{2}
be any homomorphism such that
T_{l}f=0
. We want to show that
f=0
. Assume that
\kappa
is algebraically closed. Then, for any integer
n>0
, the morphism
f^{sab}:\mathcal{G}^{\natural}_{\mathcal{Q}_{1}}[l^{n}]\to\mathcal{G}^{\natural}_{\mathcal{Q}_{2}}[l^{n}]
is
0
. Then the homomorphism
f^{ab}:A_{\mathcal{Q}_{1}}[l^{n}]\to A_{\mathcal{Q}_{2}}[l^{n}]
is
0
. So
f^{ab}:A_{\mathcal{Q}_{1}}\to A_{\mathcal{Q}_{2}}
is
0
since (
2.8
) holds for abelian varieties by
[
58
, Thm. 3, p.176]
. Also, (
2.8
) holds for tori since tori over an algebraically closed field are copies of
\mathbb{G}_{m}
. We then have
f^{tr}=0
and
f^{sab}=0
.

Now we can assume that
f^{sab}=0
. Then
f^{et}(\underline{Y}_{\mathcal{Q}_{1}})\subset\ker\iota_{\mathcal{Q}_{2}}
. So for any
y_{1}\in\underline{Y}_{\mathcal{Q}_{1}}(S^{\prime})
, the projection of
(f^{et}(y_{1}),0)\in\underline{Y}_{\mathcal{Q}_{2}}\times_{S}\mathcal{G}_{\mathcal{Q}_{2}}^{\natural}(S^{\prime})
to
\underline{Y}_{\mathcal{Q}_{2}}/l^{n}\underline{Y}_{\mathcal{Q}_{1}}(S^{\prime})
lies in
\mathcal{Q}_{2}[l^{n}]
. But since this projection is trivial for all integers
n>0
,
f^{et}(y_{1})
is trivial. Hence,
f^{et}
and
f^{sab}
are both trivial, so is
f
.
∎

Definition 2.16
.

Let
\mathcal{Q}
be a
1
-motive defined as in Definition
2.5
. A
polarization
of a 1-motive
\mathcal{Q}
is a homomorphism
\bm{\lambda}:\mathcal{Q}\to\mathcal{Q}^{\vee}
such that
\bm{\lambda}^{et}=\phi
for some injective homomorphism
\phi:\underline{Y}\to\underline{X}
with finite cokernel, such that
\bm{\lambda}^{tr}=\phi^{\vee}
, and such that
\bm{\lambda}^{ab}=\lambda_{A}
for some polarization
\lambda_{A}
of
A
.

A pair
(\mathcal{Q},\bm{\lambda})
of a 1-motive
\mathcal{Q}
with a polarization
\bm{\lambda}
is called a polarized 1-motive. Given a
\bm{\lambda}
, by definition,
c\circ\phi=\bm{\lambda}^{ab}\circ c^{\vee}
.
Then we have a trivialization
\varphi:=(\mathrm{Id}\times\phi)^{*}\tau:\mathbf{1}_{\underline{Y}\times\underline{Y}}\to(\mathrm{Id}\times\phi)^{*}(c^{\vee}\times c)^{*}\mathcal{P}_{A}^{\otimes{-1}}\cong(c^{\vee}\times c^{\vee})^{*}(\mathrm{Id}\times\lambda_{A})^{*}\mathcal{P}_{A}^{\otimes{-1}}
of
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-biextensions over
\underline{Y}\times\underline{Y}
. Note that by the rigidifications of
\mathcal{P}_{A}
along
e_{A}\times A^{\vee}
and
A\times e_{A^{\vee}}
, and by partial multiplication laws of biextensions (see
[
23
, VII, Déf. 2.1]
), if we replace
S
with some étale cover such that
\underline{X}=X
and
\underline{Y}=Y
are constant, then
\tau(Y,0)=\tau(0,X)=1
, and
\tau(-,-)
(resp.
\varphi(-,-)
) is bilinear over
Y\times X
(resp.
Y\times Y
).

2.2.2.

Next, we recall the definition of biextension of
1
-motives by commutative groups. Using this notion, we recall the definition of Weil pairings for
1
-motives following Deligne
[
18
, 10.2.5]
.

Definition 2.17

(
[
18
, 10.2.1]
)
.

Let
\mathcal{Q}_{1}
and
\mathcal{Q}_{2}
be two
1
-motives over
S
. Let
H
be a commutative group scheme over
S
. A
biextension
\mathcal{P}
of
(\mathcal{Q}_{1},\mathcal{Q}_{2})
by
H
is a biextension
\mathcal{P}
of
(\mathcal{G}^{\natural}_{1},\mathcal{G}^{\natural}_{2})
by
H
such that

(1)

There is a trivialization
\varrho_{1}
(resp.
\varrho_{2}
) of biextensions
\mathcal{P}|_{\mathcal{G}^{\natural}_{1}\times\underline{Y}_{2}}
(resp.
\mathcal{P}|_{\underline{Y}_{1}\times\mathcal{G}^{\natural}_{2}}
)
\varrho_{1}:\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{G}^{\natural}_{1}\times\underline{Y}_{2}}\to\mathcal{P}|_{\mathcal{G}^{\natural}_{1}\times\underline{Y}_{2}}^{\otimes{-1}}
(resp.
\varrho_{2}:\mathbf{1}_{\underline{Y}_{1}\times\mathcal{G}^{\natural}_{2}}\to\mathcal{P}|_{\underline{Y}_{1}\times\mathcal{G}^{\natural}_{2}}^{\otimes{-1}}
).

(2)

\varrho_{1}
and
\varrho_{2}
coincide when they are pulled back to
\underline{Y}_{1}\times\underline{Y}_{2}
under
(\iota_{1}\times\mathrm{Id}_{\underline{Y}_{2}})
and
(\mathrm{Id}_{\underline{Y}_{1}}\times\iota_{2})
, respectively.

Remark 2.18
.

Recall that a
1
-motive
\mathcal{Q}
can also be interpreted as a complex of group schemes
\mathcal{Q}=[\underline{Y}\xrightarrow{\ \iota\ }\mathcal{G}^{\natural}]
; here, we let
\underline{Y}
be in degree
-1
and let
\mathcal{G}^{\natural}
be in degree
0
. As remarked in
[
18
, 10.2.1]
,
\mathrm{Biext}^{1}(\mathcal{Q}_{1},\mathcal{Q}_{2};H)
, the isomorphism classes of biextensions of
(\mathcal{Q}_{1},\mathcal{Q}_{2})
by
H
, is isomorphic to
\underline{\Ext}^{1}(\mathcal{Q}_{1}\otimes^{L}\mathcal{Q}_{2},H)
; by hom-tensor adjunction
[
71
,
Lem. 0A65
]
, this group is isomorphic to
\underline{\Ext}^{1}(\mathcal{Q}_{1},R\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}(\mathcal{Q}_{2},H)).

Lemma 2.19
.

Let
\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{Q}}
be the pullback of
\mathcal{P}_{A}
to
\mathcal{G}^{\natural}\times\mathcal{G}^{\natural,\vee}
. Then
\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{Q}}
can be canonically viewed as a biextension of
(\mathcal{Q},\mathcal{Q}^{\vee})
by
\mathbb{G}_{m}
.

Proof.

Let
S^{\prime}
be any
S
-scheme such that
\underline{X}(S^{\prime})=X
and
\underline{Y}(S^{\prime})=Y
. Giving a section
x\in\mathcal{G}^{\natural}(S^{\prime})
is equivalent to giving a section
x_{A}\in A(S^{\prime})
together with a section
x_{f}
of the fiber
\mathcal{G}^{\natural}|_{x_{A}}
over
S^{\prime}
; a section
x_{f}
of the trivial
T
-torsor
\mathcal{G}^{\natural}|_{x_{A}}
is determined by a map
\chi\in X(S^{\prime})\mapsto x_{f}(\chi)\in\mathcal{O}_{\chi}|_{x_{A}}(S^{\prime})
(here
\mathcal{O}_{\chi}
means the corresponding
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-bundle), a section of the pushout of
\mathcal{G}^{\natural}|_{x_{A}}
along
-\chi
. We show that
\varrho_{1}(x,\chi):=x_{f}(\chi)^{-1}
is the desired trivialization of the biextension
\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{Q}}^{\otimes-1}|_{\mathcal{G}^{\natural}\times\underline{X}}
.

To see this, we have to show that
\varrho_{1}(-,-)
can be canonically viewed as a trivialization of
\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{Q}}|_{(x,\chi)}
for any
x\in\mathcal{G}^{\natural}(S^{\prime})
and any
\chi\in X(S^{\prime})
, and that
\varrho_{1}(-,-)
is bilinear. We let
S=S^{\prime}
.
Note that any
x\in\mathcal{G}^{\natural}(S)
induces a section
x\in\mathcal{G}^{\natural}\times_{A}\mathcal{G}^{\natural}(S)
under the diagonal embedding
\Delta:\mathcal{G}^{\natural}\to\mathcal{G}^{\natural}\times_{A}\mathcal{G}^{\natural}
. Then
\varrho_{1}(x,\chi)
is defined by (the inverse of) the pushout of
\mathcal{G}^{\natural}\times_{A}\mathcal{G}^{\natural}|_{(x,x)}
from the second factor along
-\chi
. The case for
\varrho_{2}(-,-)
is constructed symmetrically.

Next, let us explain the bilinearity. The linearity with respect to the first factor follows from the multiplicative structure of
\mathcal{G}^{\natural}
and the diagonal embedding; the linearity of the second factor follows from the multiplicative structure of pushouts with respect to the tensor product:
\mathcal{O}_{\chi}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\chi^{\prime}}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathcal{O}_{\chi+\chi^{\prime}}
.

Finally,
\varrho_{1}
and
\varrho_{2}
coincide when pulled back to
Y\times X
: this can be seen from the equivalence of
\tau
,
\iota
and
\iota^{\vee}
; see
2.2.1
,
4
,

′

‣
2.2.1
and

′′

‣
2.2.1
.
∎

We will keep on using the notation
\varrho_{1}(g,h)
(resp.
\varrho_{2}(g,h)
) defined in Lemma
2.19
, for
g\in\mathcal{G}^{\natural}(S^{\prime})
and
h\in\iota^{\vee}(\underline{X})(S^{\prime})
(resp. for
g\in\iota(\underline{Y})(S^{\prime})
and
h\in\mathcal{G}^{\natural,\vee}
), where
S^{\prime}
is an
S
-scheme; if there is more than one
1
-motive involved, we will use
\varrho_{1}^{\mathcal{Q}}
and
\varrho_{2}^{\mathcal{Q}}
to specify that the trivializations
\varrho_{1}
and
\varrho_{2}
are defined for
\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{Q}}
. If
g
(resp.
h
) is trivial, we denote
\mathrm{rig}_{h}:=\varrho_{2}(0,h)
(resp.
\mathrm{rig}_{g}:=\varrho_{1}(g,0)
).

Now we recall Deligne’s definition of Weil pairings.

For any two valued points
x\in\mathcal{G}^{\natural}(S^{\prime})
and
y\in\mathcal{G}^{\natural,\vee}(S^{\prime})
for some
S
-scheme
S^{\prime}
, we denote
\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{Q}}|_{(x,y)}
by
\mathcal{P}_{x,y}
. Moreover, we denote
(x\times\mathrm{Id}_{\mathcal{G}^{\natural,\vee}})^{*}\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{Q}}
(resp.
(\mathrm{Id}_{\mathcal{G}^{\natural}}\times y)^{*}\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{Q}}
) by
\mathcal{O}_{x}
(resp.
\mathcal{O}_{y}
).

Definition 2.20

(
[
18
, 10.2.5]
)
.

The
Weil pairing for a
1
-motive
\mathcal{Q}
over
S
is a perfect pairing
{\mathrm{e}}_{\mathcal{Q}[n]}:\mathcal{Q}[n]\times\mathcal{Q}^{\vee}[n]\to\mathbb{G}_{m}
, which is defined as the difference of two trivializations described as follows:

Let
q_{1}\in\mathcal{Q}[n](S^{\prime})
and
q_{2}\in\mathcal{Q}^{\vee}[n](S^{\prime})
. Suppose that
q_{1}
lifts to
(y,g)\in\underline{Y}(S^{\prime})\times\mathcal{G}^{\natural}(S^{\prime})
and
q_{2}
lifts to
(x,h)\in\underline{X}(S^{\prime})\times\mathcal{G}^{\natural,\vee}(S^{\prime})
. Then
\mathrm{e}_{\mathcal{Q}[n]}(q_{1},q_{2})
fits into the commutative diagram

(2.9)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{P}_{\iota(y),h}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{G}_{m}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{P}^{\otimes n}_{g,h}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{P}_{g,\iota^{\vee}(x)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{G}_{m}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varrho_{2}(y,h)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{can.}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{can.}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varrho_{1}(g,x)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{e}_{\mathcal{Q}[n]}(q_{1},q_{2})}

Symbolically, we write
{\mathrm{e}}_{\mathcal{Q}[n]}(q_{1},q_{2}):=\frac{\varrho_{2}(y,h)}{\varrho_{1}(g,x)}
.

Moreover, for any polarization
\bm{\lambda}
of
\mathcal{Q}
,
\bm{\lambda}(\mathcal{Q}[n])\subset\mathcal{Q}^{\vee}[n]
(see Lemma
2.11
). Define
{\mathrm{e}}^{\bm{\lambda}}:\mathcal{Q}[n]\times\mathcal{Q}[n]\to\mathbb{G}_{m}
to be
{\mathrm{e}}^{\bm{\lambda}}(q_{1},q_{2}):={\mathrm{e}}_{\mathcal{Q}[n]}(q_{1},\bm{\lambda}(q_{2}))
for
q_{1},q_{2}\in\mathcal{Q}[n]
.

The following lemma is immediate:

Lemma 2.21
.

Let
(\mathcal{Q},\bm{\lambda})
be a polarized
1
-motive and let
f:\mathcal{Q}_{1}\to\mathcal{Q}
be an isogeny between
1
-motives. Then
\bm{\lambda}_{1}:=f^{\vee}\circ\bm{\lambda}\circ f
is a polarization of
\mathcal{Q}_{1}
.

Let
q_{1}\in\mathcal{Q}_{1}[n]
and
q_{2}\in\mathcal{Q}^{\vee}[n]
. Set
q_{1}=(y,g)\in\underline{Y}_{\mathcal{Q}_{1}}(S^{\prime})\times\mathcal{G}^{\natural}_{\mathcal{Q}_{1}}(S^{\prime})
and
q_{2}=(x,h)\in\underline{X}_{\mathcal{Q}}(S^{\prime})\times\mathcal{G}^{\natural,\vee}_{\mathcal{Q}}(S^{\prime})
. Then
\varrho_{1}^{\mathcal{Q}}(f^{sab}(g),x)=\varrho_{1}^{\mathcal{Q}_{1}}(g,f^{\vee,et}(x))
, and

\varrho_{2}^{\mathcal{Q}}(f^{et}(y),h)=\varrho_{2}^{\mathcal{Q}_{1}}(y,f^{\vee,sab}(h))
.

Consequently, we have the following equalities:
\mathrm{e}_{\mathcal{Q}[n]}(f(q_{1}),q_{2})=\mathrm{e}_{\mathcal{Q}_{1}[n]}(q_{1},f^{\vee}(q_{2}))
; if
q_{1},q_{2}\in\mathcal{Q}_{1}[n](S^{\prime})
,
\mathrm{e}^{\bm{\lambda}}(f(q_{1}),f(q_{2}))=\mathrm{e}^{\bm{\lambda}_{1}}(q_{1},q_{2})
; for any
y_{1}\in\underline{Y}_{\mathcal{Q}_{1}}(S^{\prime})
and any
x\in\underline{X}_{\mathcal{Q}}(S^{\prime})
,
\tau_{\mathcal{Q}}(f^{\text{{\'{e}}t}}(y),x)=\tau_{\mathcal{Q}_{1}}(y,f^{\vee,et}(x))
.

Proof.

The first paragraph follows directly from the definition. The second paragraph follows from the construction of
\varrho_{1}
and
\varrho_{2}
in Lemma
2.19
, as the pushout of
g\in\mathcal{G}^{\natural}_{\mathcal{Q}_{1}}(S^{\prime})
under
-f^{\vee,et}(x)
factors through the pushout of
f^{sab}(g)\in\mathcal{G}^{\natural}_{\mathcal{Q}}(S^{\prime})
under
-x
, and as the pushout of
f^{\vee,sab}(h)\in\mathcal{G}^{\natural,\vee}_{\mathcal{Q}_{1}}(S^{\prime})
under
-y
factors through the pushout of
h\in\mathcal{G}^{\natural,\vee}_{\mathcal{Q}}(S^{\prime})
under
-f^{et}(y)
. The third paragraph follows from the second paragraph.
∎

2.2.3.

The statements in §
2.2.3
and §
2.2.4
are not used in the remaining part of this paper. These two subsections are retained to ensure consistency with the submitted version of the thesis.

We make some explanations on how the definitions above are compatible with the general theory of Ext- and Biext- groups studied in
[
23
, VII and VIII]
.

First, let us consider the complex
[\underline{Y}\xrightarrow{\ c^{\vee}\ }A]
concentrated in degrees
-1
and
0
. Then, as in
[
23
, VII, 3.1 and Thm. 3.2.5]
, the group
\underline{\Ext}^{1}([\underline{Y}\to A],\mathbb{G}_{m})
can be described as follows:

Theorem 2.22

(
[
23
, VII, Thm. 3.2.5]
)
.

Let
L=[\cdots\rightarrow L_{2}\xrightarrow{d_{2}}L_{1}\xrightarrow{d_{1}}L_{0}\rightarrow 0]
be a complex of fppf sheaves of commutative groups over
S
such that
L_{0}
is in degree
0
. Let
H
be a commutative group over
S
. Then

\underline{\Ext}^{1}(L,H)\cong\{\begin{array}[]{c}(E,\alpha)|E\text{ is an extension of }L_{0}\text{ by }H,\ \alpha\text{ is a trivialization}\\
\text{of the pullback extension }d_{1}^{*}(E)\text{ such that }\alpha\circ d_{2}=0\end{array}\}/\cong,

where an isomorphism
f:(E,\alpha)\to(E^{\prime},\alpha^{\prime})
is an isomorphism of extensions of
L_{0}
by
H
,
f:E\to E^{\prime}
, such that
f\circ\alpha=\alpha^{\prime}
. Moreover,
\underline{\Ext}^{0}(L,H)\cong\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}_{S-gp}(H^{0}(L),H)
.

As there is an exact sequence

0\to[0\rightarrow A]\to[\underline{Y}\rightarrow A]\to[\underline{Y}\rightarrow 0]\to 0,

applying
R\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}(-,\mathbb{G}_{m})
, we see that there is an exact sequence

0\to T^{\vee}\to\underline{\Ext}^{1}([\underline{Y}\to A],\mathbb{G}_{m})\to A^{\vee}\to 0,

and
R^{0}\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}([\underline{Y}\to A],\mathbb{G}_{m})=0
.

On the other hand, since the arguments in
[
23
, VIII, 1.1.4 and 1.4.2]
involve only general homological algebra and also work in the case of
1
-motives, and since
R^{0}\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}([\underline{Y}\to A],\mathbb{G}_{m})
is trivial, we have an isomorphism
\biext^{1}(\mathcal{T},[\underline{Y}\xrightarrow{c^{\vee}}A];\mathbb{G}_{m})\xrightarrow{\sim}\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}(\mathcal{T},\underline{\Ext}^{1}([\underline{Y}\xrightarrow{c^{\vee}}A],\mathbb{G}_{m}))
for any fppf sheaf of commutative groups
\mathcal{T}
. Then the functor
\biext^{1}(-,[\underline{Y}\to A];\mathbb{G}_{m})
from
Sh(S_{fppf})^{\mathrm{op}}
to
Sh(S_{fppf})
is represented by
\underline{\Ext}^{1}([\underline{Y}\to A],\mathbb{G}_{m})
; the fppf sheaf
\underline{\Ext}^{1}([\underline{Y}\to A],\mathbb{G}_{m})
is representable by a commutative group scheme by fppf descent of affine morphisms (see
[
62
, Prop. 17.4]
; see also
[
71
,
Lem. 0247
]
).

More precisely,

Proposition 2.23
.

\underline{\Ext}^{1}([\underline{Y}\to A],\mathbb{G}_{m})
is representable by
\mathcal{G}^{\natural,\vee}
. In particular, if we assume that
\underline{Y}
splits, for simplicity, then
\underline{\Ext}^{1}([\underline{Y}\to A],\mathbb{G}_{m})\cong\bigoplus_{y\in Y}\mathcal{O}_{c^{\vee}(y)}
, where
\mathcal{O}_{c^{\vee}(y)}:=(c^{\vee}(y)\times\mathrm{Id}_{A^{\vee}})^{*}\mathcal{P}_{A}
. Similar statements are true if we replace
c^{\vee},\underline{X}
and
A
with
c
,
\underline{Y}
and
A^{\vee}
, respectively.

Proof.

Let
\mathcal{P}
be the
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-extension over
A\times\mathcal{G}^{\natural,\vee}
defined by the pullback of
\mathcal{P}_{A}
under
\mathrm{Id}_{A}\times\pi^{\vee}
. Then as Lemma
2.19
, the diagonal embedding
\Delta:\mathcal{G}^{\natural,\vee}\to\mathcal{G}^{\natural,\vee}\times_{A^{\vee}}\mathcal{G}^{\natural,\vee}
defines a canonical trivialization of
\mathbf{t}^{\Delta}:\mathbf{1}_{\underline{Y}\times\mathcal{G}^{\natural,\vee}}\to(c^{\vee}\times\mathrm{Id}_{\mathcal{G}^{\natural,\vee}})^{*}\mathcal{P}
over
\underline{Y}\times\mathcal{G}^{\natural,\vee}
by pushing out sections
-y\in\underline{Y}(S^{\prime})
for any
S
-scheme
S^{\prime}
; we claim that
(\mathcal{P},\mathbf{t}^{\Delta})
is the tautological biextension (with the sign of trivializations opposite to the definition appearing previously) of the functor
\biext^{1}(-,[\underline{Y}\to A];\mathbb{G}_{m})
.

On the one hand, for any commutative group scheme
H
over
S
, any
x\in\mathcal{G}^{\natural,\vee}(H)
, the pair
((\mathrm{Id}_{A}\times x)^{*}\mathcal{P},(\mathrm{Id}_{A}\times x)^{*}\mathbf{t}^{\Delta})
defines a desired biextension in
\biext^{1}(H,[\underline{Y}\xrightarrow{c^{\vee}}A];\mathbb{G}_{m})
. On the other hand, for any biextension
(E,\alpha)
of
([\underline{Y}\to A],H)
by
\mathbb{G}_{m}
, the image of
(E,\alpha)
from the top-left corner to the bottom-right corner of the commutative diagram

(2.10)
