{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\biext^{1}(H,[\underline{Y}\xrightarrow{c^{\vee}}A];\mathbb{G}_{m})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}(H,\underline{\Ext}^{1}([\underline{Y}\xrightarrow{c^{\vee}}A],\mathbb{G}_{m}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\biext^{1}(H,A;\mathbb{G}_{m})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}(H,A^{\vee})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{can.}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{can.}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}

determines a homomorphism
f_{E}:H\to A^{\vee}
, and this induces an isomorphism
E\cong(\mathrm{Id}_{A}\times f_{E})^{*}\mathcal{P}_{A}
. Then
\alpha
is a trivialization that assigns each
y\in\underline{Y}(S^{\prime})
a trivialization
\alpha(y)
of
f_{E}^{*}\mathcal{O}_{c^{\vee}(y)}(S^{\prime})
for any
S
-scheme
S^{\prime}
, and this is equivalent to a homomorphism
a:H\to H\times_{A^{\vee}}\mathcal{G}^{\natural,\vee}
. This proves the claim.
∎

With the definition above, we can then construct
\iota^{\vee}
from
\iota
by pushing out
\mathcal{G}^{\natural}
along
-\chi
. By doing this, there is a
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-extension
\mathcal{O}_{\chi}
with a section
\underline{Y}\to\mathcal{G}^{\natural}\xrightarrow{-\chi}\mathcal{O}_{\chi}
for any
\chi
, which induces a map
\iota^{\vee}:\underline{X}\to\mathcal{G}^{\natural,\vee}
by the proposition above.

2.2.4.

The following lemma is self-explanatory due to the definition of
n
-torsion points.

Lemma 2.24

(See also
[
21
, III., Cor. 7.3]
)
.

Recall that there are two extensions induced by the weight filtration of
\mathcal{Q}[n]

(2.11)

\begin{split}0\longrightarrow\mathcal{G}^{\natural}[n]\longrightarrow\mathcal{Q}[n]\longrightarrow\underline{Y}/n\underline{Y}\longrightarrow 0,\\
0\longrightarrow T[n]\longrightarrow\mathcal{G}^{\natural}[n]\longrightarrow A[n]\longrightarrow 0.\end{split}

The first extension is determined by the image of
\iota
under the canonical connecting homomorphism
\partial:\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}(\underline{Y},\mathcal{G}^{\natural})\to\underline{\Ext}^{1}(\underline{Y},\mathcal{G}^{\natural}[n])=\underline{\Ext}^{1}(\underline{Y}/n\underline{Y},\mathcal{G}^{\natural}[n])
induced by applying
R\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}(\underline{Y},-)
to
0\to\mathcal{G}^{\natural}[n]\to\mathcal{G}^{\natural}\to\mathcal{G}^{\natural}\to 0
; the second extension is the dual of the extension determined by the image of
c
under the canonical connecting homomorphism
\partial:\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}(\underline{X},A^{\vee})\to\underline{\Ext}^{1}(\underline{X},A^{\vee}[n])=\underline{\Ext}^{1}(\underline{X}/n\underline{X},A^{\vee}[n])
induced by applying
R\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}(\underline{X},-)
to
0\to A^{\vee}[n]\to A^{\vee}\to A^{\vee}\to 0
.

The construction of the Cartier dual
\mathcal{Q}^{\vee}
(see
[
18
, 10.2.11]
) can be viewed as the following statement:

Proposition 2.25
.

With the notation and conventions introduced in Remark
2.10
,
the Cartier dual
\mathcal{Q}^{\vee}
is isomorphic to the
1
-motive represented by
(\tau^{\leq 1}R\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}(\mathcal{Q},\mathbb{G}_{m}))\{1\}
, where
\tau^{\leq 1}
denotes the canonical truncation and where
\{-\}
denotes the shifting of complexes.

Proof.

There is an exact sequence

0\longrightarrow T\longrightarrow\mathcal{Q}\longrightarrow\mathcal{Q}^{\circ}\longrightarrow 0,

where
\mathcal{Q}^{\circ}:=[\underline{Y}\to A]
.
Applying
R\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}(-,\mathbb{G}_{m})
, there is a long exact sequence

\displaystyle 0\longrightarrow R^{0}\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}(\mathcal{Q}^{\circ},\mathbb{G}_{m})\longrightarrow R^{0}\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}(\mathcal{Q},\mathbb{G}_{m})\longrightarrow\underline{X}\xrightarrow{\ \delta\ }R^{1}\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}(\mathcal{Q}^{\circ},\mathbb{G}_{m})\longrightarrow

\displaystyle R^{1}\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}(\mathcal{Q},\mathbb{G}_{m})\longrightarrow 0.

The first term
R^{0}\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}(\mathcal{Q}^{\circ},\mathbb{G}_{m})
is trivial by the second paragraph before Proposition
2.23
; moreover, the fourth term
R^{1}\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}(\mathcal{Q}^{\circ},\mathbb{G}_{m})=\underline{\Ext}^{1}([\underline{Y}\xrightarrow{c^{\vee}}A],\mathbb{G}_{m})\cong\mathcal{G}^{\natural,\vee}
by Proposition
2.23
. Moreover,
\delta=-\iota^{\vee}
since
\delta
is constructed by pushing out.

There is a distinguished triangle

R\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}(\mathcal{Q}^{\circ},\mathbb{G}_{m})\longrightarrow R\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}(\mathcal{Q},\mathbb{G}_{m})\longrightarrow R\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}(T,\mathbb{G}_{m})\xrightarrow[+1]{\delta};

this triangle shifts to a distinguished triangle

R\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}(T,\mathbb{G}_{m})\{-1\}\xrightarrow{\ -\delta\{-1\}\ }R\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}(\mathcal{Q}^{\circ},\mathbb{G}_{m})\longrightarrow R\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}(\mathcal{Q},\mathbb{G}_{m})\xrightarrow{+1}.

From Lemma
2.26
, since
R^{1}\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}(T,\mathbb{G}_{m})=\underline{\Ext}^{1}(T,\mathbb{G}_{m})
is trivial, there is a distinguished triangle

\tau^{\leq 1}(R\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}(T,\mathbb{G}_{m}))\xrightarrow{\ -\delta\ }(\tau^{\leq 1}R\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}(\mathcal{Q}^{\circ},\mathbb{G}_{m}))\{1\}\longrightarrow(\tau^{\leq 1}R\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}(\mathcal{Q},\mathbb{G}_{m}))\{1\}\xrightarrow{+1}.

Since
\tau^{\leq 1}R\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}(T,\mathbb{G}_{m})\cong\underline{X}
and
(\tau^{\leq 1}R\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}(\mathcal{Q}^{\circ},\mathbb{G}_{m}))\{1\}\cong\mathcal{G}^{\natural,\vee}
,
(\tau^{\leq 1}R\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}(\mathcal{Q},\mathbb{G}_{m}))\{1\}\cong[\underline{X}\xrightarrow{-\delta\ }\mathcal{G}^{\natural,\vee}]
, which is concentrated in degrees
-1
and
0
.
∎

Lemma 2.26
.

Let
f:A^{\bullet}\to B^{\bullet}
be a morphism between complexes of fppf sheaves of commutative groups over
S
. Assume that
A^{i}=B^{i}=0
for any
i<0
, and assume that
H^{2}(A^{\bullet})=0
. Let
\mathcal{C}_{f}
be the mapping cone of
f
. Then
\tau^{\leq 1}\mathcal{C}_{f}
is quasi-isomorphic to the mapping cone of
{\tau^{\leq 1}}f:\tau^{\leq 1}A^{\bullet}\to\tau^{\leq 1}B^{\bullet}
.

Proof.

Write
A^{\bullet}=[0\to A^{0}\to A^{1}\to A^{2}\to\cdots]
and
B^{\bullet}=[0\to B^{0}\to B^{1}\to B^{2}\to\cdots]
. Let
K^{1}:=\im(d_{A}^{1}:A^{1}\to A^{2})
and
R^{1}:=\im(d_{B}^{1}:B^{1}\to B^{2})
. By assumption,
K^{1}=\ker(d^{2}_{A}:A^{2}\to A^{3})
. Then the mapping cone
\mathcal{C}_{f}
of
f
can be written as
(\mathcal{C}_{f}^{i},d_{C}^{i})
, where
\mathcal{C}_{f}^{i}=A^{i+1}\oplus B^{i}
and
d_{C}^{i}=(-d_{A}^{i+1},f^{i+1}+d_{B}^{i})
; the first four terms can be written as
[0\to A^{0}\to A^{1}\oplus B^{0}\to A^{2}\oplus B^{1}\to A^{3}\oplus B^{2}\to\cdots]
, where
A^{0}
is concentrated in degree
-1
.

Then
\tau^{\leq 1}\mathcal{C}_{f}=[0\to A^{0}\to A^{1}\oplus B^{0}\to A^{2}\oplus B^{1}\to\im(d_{C}^{1}:\mathcal{C}_{f}^{1}\to\mathcal{C}_{f}^{2})\to 0]
. On the other hand, the mapping cone of
[0\to A^{0}\to A^{1}\to K^{1}\to 0]\to[0\to B^{0}\to B^{1}\to R^{1}\to 0]
is
\mathcal{C}_{\tau^{\leq 1}f}:=[0\to A^{0}\to A^{1}\oplus B^{0}\to K^{1}\oplus B^{1}\to 0\oplus R^{1}\to 0]
. There is an obvious morphism
\omega:\mathcal{C}_{\tau^{\leq 1}f}\to\tau^{\leq 1}\mathcal{C}_{f}
induced by inclusions
K^{1}\to A^{2}
and
R^{1}\to\im(d_{C}^{1}:\mathcal{C}_{f}^{1}\to\mathcal{C}_{f}^{2})
. We can check that
\omega
is a quasi-isomorphism: We need to check the isomorphism of
H^{1}
. If
d_{C}^{1}(a_{2},b_{1})=0
for some
a_{2}\in A^{2}
and
b_{1}\in B^{1}
, then
d_{A}^{2}(a_{2})=0
, so
a_{2}\in K^{1}
by assumption. Since the image of
d_{C}^{0}
factors through
K^{1}\oplus B^{1}
, we deduce that
H^{1}(\mathcal{C}_{\tau^{\leq 1}f})=H^{1}(\tau^{\leq 1}\mathcal{C}_{f})
; for the same reason, we can also see that
H^{0}(\mathcal{C}_{\tau^{\leq 1}f})=H^{0}(\tau^{\leq 1}\mathcal{C}_{f})
.
∎

Finally, the duality of
n
-torsion subgroups of
1
-motives can also be described as follows:

Proposition 2.27
.

\mathcal{Q}^{\vee}[n]\cong\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}_{1-mot}(\mathcal{Q}[n],\mathbb{G}_{m})
.

Proof.

We have the following two diagrams:

(2.12)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{Q}^{\vee}\{-1\}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{Q}^{\vee}\{-1\}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}_{n}(\mathcal{Q}^{\vee})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{Q}^{\vee}\{-1\}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}(\mathcal{Q},\mathbb{G}_{m})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{Y}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau^{>1}R\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}(\mathcal{Q},\mathbb{G}_{m})}
,
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces n}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces+1}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces+1}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces+1}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces+1}

and

(2.13)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{Q}^{\vee}\{-1\}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}(\mathcal{Q},\mathbb{G}_{m})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau^{>1}R\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}(\mathcal{Q},\mathbb{G}_{m})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{Q}^{\vee}\{-1\}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}(\mathcal{Q},\mathbb{G}_{m})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{Y}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}_{n}}
.
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces n}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces+1}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces+1}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces+1}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces+1}

From the Octahedral Axiom (TR4; see
[
71
,
Def. 0145
]
), we find that the two dashed triangles are distinguished. Then
\mathcal{Q}^{\vee}[n]\cong H^{0}(\mathcal{C}_{n}(\mathcal{Q}^{\vee}))\cong H^{0}(\mathcal{Y})\cong H^{0}(\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}_{n})
.
On the other hand, by
[
68
, Prop. 2.3.1]
,
\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}_{1-mot}(\mathcal{Q}[n],\mathbb{G}_{m})\cong R^{0}\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}(\mathcal{Q}[n],\mathbb{G}_{m})
, and
R^{0}\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}(\mathcal{Q}[n],\mathbb{G}_{m})\cong H^{0}(R\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}(\mathcal{C}_{n}(\mathcal{Q}),\mathbb{G}_{m}))\cong H^{0}(Cone(R\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}(\mathcal{Q},\mathbb{G}_{m})\xrightarrow{n}R\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}(\mathcal{Q},\mathbb{G}_{m})))\cong H^{0}(\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}_{n})
. So we have the desired result.
∎

2.3.
Theory of degeneration

We review the notions of degenerating families, degeneration data, and their level structures. Along the way, we will explain that the definitions of level structures associated with the degeneration data in
[
44
, Sec. 6.2.3]
and in
[
50
, Sec. 2.2]
are equivalent (see Proposition
2.39
). The aim of §
2.3.1
-§
2.3.4
is to provide a very brief summary; see
[
21
]
and
[
44
]
for all the details that we omit.

2.3.1.

Let
R
be a Noetherian normal domain that is complete with respect to a radical ideal
I\subset R
. Let
K:=\Frac{R}
be the fraction field. Denote
R_{i}:=R/I^{i+1}
,
\mathcal{W}:=\spec R
,
\mathcal{W}_{i}:=\spec R_{i}
and
\eta:=\spec K
. Denote
\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{for}}:=\spf(R,I)
.

Definition 2.28

(
[
44
, Def. 4.4.2]
)
.

Define the category
\mathbf{DEG}_{\mathrm{pol}}(R,I)
as follows:

(1)

An object of
\mathbf{DEG}_{\mathrm{pol}}(R,I)
is a pair
(\mathcal{G},\lambda)
of a semi-abelian scheme
\mathcal{G}
over
\mathcal{W}
with a homomorphism
\lambda:\mathcal{G}\to\mathcal{G}^{\vee}
.

(2)

The generic fiber
\mathcal{G}_{\eta}
is an abelian scheme, and
\lambda_{\eta}:\mathcal{G}_{\eta}\to\mathcal{G}_{\eta}^{\vee}
is a polarization of the abelian scheme
\mathcal{G}_{\eta}
. The pullback
\mathcal{G}_{0}:=\mathcal{G}\times_{\mathcal{W}}\mathcal{W}_{0}
is an extension of an abelian scheme
A_{0}
by an isotrivial torus
T_{0}
over
\mathcal{W}_{0}
.

(3)

A morphism
f:(\mathcal{G}_{1},\lambda_{1})\to(\mathcal{G}_{2},\lambda_{2})
in
\mathbf{DEG}_{\mathrm{pol}}(R,I)
is a homomorphism
f
from
\mathcal{G}_{1}
to
\mathcal{G}_{2}
respecting the given polarizations over
\eta
, i.e.,
f^{\vee}_{\eta}\circ\lambda_{2,\eta}\circ f_{\eta}=\lambda_{1,\eta}
.

Let
(V_{\mathbb{Z}},\psi_{\mathbb{Z}})
be as in §
2.1
. Let
S
be a normal locally Noetherian scheme, and let
U
be a dense open subscheme of
S
.

Definition 2.29

(
[
44
, Def. 5.3.2.1]
)
.

Define the groupoid
\mathbf{DEG}_{(V_{\mathbb{Z}},\psi_{\mathbb{Z}}),K^{{\ddagger},\square}}(S,U)
of
degenerating families of type
\mathbf{M}^{\mathrm{iso}}_{(V_{\mathbb{Z}},\psi_{\mathbb{Z}}),K^{{\ddagger},\square}}
as follows:

(1)

An object in
\mathbf{DEG}_{(V_{\mathbb{Z}},\psi_{\mathbb{Z}}),K^{{\ddagger},\square}}(S,U)
is a tuple
(\mathcal{G},\lambda,[\varepsilon^{{\ddagger}}_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{\square}}]_{K^{{\ddagger},\square}})
, where
\mathcal{G}
is a semi-abelian scheme over
S
and
\lambda:\mathcal{G}\to\mathcal{G}^{\vee}
is a homomorphism. The pullback
(\mathcal{G}_{U},\lambda_{U})
is a principally polarized abelian scheme over
U
such that
[\varepsilon^{{\ddagger}}_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{\square}}]_{K^{{\ddagger},\square}}
is an integral level structure for
(\mathcal{G}_{U},\lambda_{U})
of the moduli problem
\mathbf{M}^{\mathrm{iso}}_{(V_{\mathbb{Z}},\psi_{\mathbb{Z}}),K^{{\ddagger},\square}}
. That is, the pullback
(\mathcal{G}_{U},\lambda_{U},[\varepsilon^{{\ddagger}}_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{\square}}]_{K^{{\ddagger},\square}})
is an object in
\mathbf{M}^{\mathrm{iso}}_{(V_{\mathbb{Z}},\psi_{\mathbb{Z}}),K^{{\ddagger},\square}}(U)
.

(2)

Two tuples
(\mathcal{G},\lambda,[\varepsilon^{{\ddagger}}_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{\square}}]_{K^{{\ddagger},\square}})
and
(\mathcal{G}^{\prime},\lambda^{\prime},[\varepsilon^{{\ddagger},\prime}_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{\square}}]_{K^{{\ddagger},\square}})
are equivalent if and only if
(\mathcal{G}_{U},\lambda_{U},[\varepsilon^{{\ddagger}}_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{\square}}]_{K^{{\ddagger},\square}})\cong(\mathcal{G}^{\prime}_{U},\lambda^{\prime}_{U},[\varepsilon^{{\ddagger},\prime}_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{\square}}]_{K^{{\ddagger},\square}})
in
\mathbf{M}^{\mathrm{iso}}_{(V_{\mathbb{Z}},\psi_{\mathbb{Z}}),K^{{\ddagger},\square}}(U)
.

2.3.2.

Definition 2.30

(
[
44
, p.212]
)
.

Let
\mathcal{Q}
be a
1
-motive. Define
\bm{\lambda}:=(\phi,\lambda_{A})
to be a pair consisting of an injective homomorphism
\phi:\underline{Y}\to\underline{X}
with finite cokernel, and a polarization
\lambda_{A}:A\to A^{\vee}
of
A=\mathcal{Q}^{ab}
, such that
\lambda_{A}\circ c^{\vee}=c\circ\phi
. With conventions introduced in Definition
2.16
and the paragraph below it,
we say that some trivialization
\tau
of biextension
(c^{\vee}\times c)^{*}\mathcal{P}_{A}^{\otimes-1}
is symmetric (with respect to
\bm{\lambda}
) if, for any étale
S
-scheme
S^{\prime}
and any
y,y^{\prime}\in\underline{Y}(S^{\prime})
, we have
\varphi(y,y^{\prime})=\varphi(y^{\prime},y)
, where
\varphi:=(\mathrm{Id}\times\phi)^{*}\tau
.

Lemma 2.31
.

Let
\tau=\tau_{\mathcal{Q}}
be the trivialization of
\mathcal{P}_{A}^{\otimes-1}
canonically constructed from
\iota_{\mathcal{Q}}
. Let
\bm{\lambda}
be a polarization of
\mathcal{Q}
. Then
\tau
is symmetric with respect to
(\bm{\lambda}^{et},\bm{\lambda}^{ab})
.

Proof.

This follows from Definition
2.16
and the last statement of Lemma
2.21
.
∎

Definition 2.32

(
[
44
, Def. 4.4.6]
)
.

Define the category
\mathbf{DD}_{\mathrm{pol}}(R,I)
of degeneration data over
(R,I)
(without level structures) as follows:

(1)

An object in
\mathbf{DD}_{\mathrm{pol}}(R,I)
is a polarized 1-motive
(\mathcal{Q}_{\eta},\bm{\lambda}_{\eta})
over
\eta
. Moreover, we denote
?^{\sharp}_{\eta}:=?^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{Q}_{\eta}}
for
?=\underline{Y},\underline{X},\mathcal{G}^{\natural},T,A,c,\iota,\tau
and for
\sharp=\emptyset,\vee
. Then
\underline{X}_{\eta}
(resp.
\underline{Y}_{\eta}
) extends to an étale locally finite free
\mathbb{Z}
-module
\underline{X}
(resp.
\underline{Y}
) over
\mathcal{W}
;
\mathcal{G}^{\natural}_{\eta}
(resp.
G^{\natural,\vee}_{\eta}
) extends to a semi-abelian scheme
\mathcal{G}^{\natural}
(resp.
G^{\natural,\vee}
) over
\mathcal{W}
, which is an extension of abelian scheme
A
(resp.
A^{\vee}
) extending
A_{\eta}
(resp.
A^{\vee}_{\eta}
) by a torus
T
(resp.
T^{\vee}
) extending
T_{\eta}
(resp.
T^{\vee}_{\eta}
);
c_{\eta}
(resp.
c^{\vee}_{\eta}
) extends to a homomorphism
c
(resp.
c^{\vee}
). Consequently,
\bm{\lambda}^{et}_{\eta}=\phi_{\eta}
and
\bm{\lambda}_{\eta}^{ab}=\lambda_{A,\eta}
canonically extend to homomorphisms
\bm{\lambda}^{et}=\phi:\underline{Y}\to\underline{X}
and
\bm{\lambda}^{ab}=\lambda_{A}:A\to A^{\vee}
.

(2)

The trivialization
\tau_{\eta}
satisfies the
positivity conditions
, i.e., after replacing
\mathcal{W}
with an étale cover of
\mathcal{W}
such that
\underline{Y}
splits, for any
y\in\underline{Y}
,
\tau_{\eta}(y,\phi(y))
extends to a section of
(\mathrm{Id}\times\phi)^{*}(c^{\vee}\times c)^{*}\mathcal{P}_{A}^{\otimes{-1}}
over
\mathcal{W}
, and this section factors through
I
if
y
is nonzero. Moreover,
\tau_{\eta}
is
necessarily
symmetric with respect to
(\phi_{\eta},\lambda_{A,\eta})
(see Lemma
2.31
).

(3)

A morphism
f:(\mathcal{Q}_{\eta,1},\bm{\lambda}_{\eta,1})\to(\mathcal{Q}_{\eta,2},\bm{\lambda}_{\eta,2})
in
\mathbf{DD}_{\mathrm{pol}}(R,I)
is a homomorphism
f:\mathcal{Q}_{\eta,1}\to\mathcal{Q}_{\eta,2}
respecting the given polarizations, i.e.,
f^{\vee}\circ\bm{\lambda}_{\eta,2}\circ f=\bm{\lambda}_{\eta,1}
.

Construction 2.33

(
[
21
, Ch. II]
and
[
44
, Sec. 4.1-4.3]
)
.

Let us recall the construction of the functor
\mathbf{F}_{\mathrm{pol}}(R,I)
that associates with any object
(\mathcal{G},\lambda)
in
\mathbf{DEG}_{\mathrm{pol}}(R,I)
an object
(\mathcal{Q}_{\eta},\bm{\lambda}_{\eta})
in
\mathbf{DD}_{\mathrm{pol}}(R,I)
:

(1)

First, we can write
\mathcal{G}_{0}:=\mathcal{G}_{\mathcal{W}_{0}}
as an extension of an abelian scheme
A_{0}
by an isotrivial torus
T_{0}
over
\mathcal{W}_{0}
:
1\to T_{0}\to\mathcal{G}_{0}\to A_{0}\to 1
. Then
T_{0}
uniquely extends to a torus
T_{i}
over
\mathcal{W}_{i}
and
T_{i}
induces an extension:
0\to T_{i}\to\mathcal{G}_{i}\to A_{i}\to 0
, where
\mathcal{G}_{i}:=\mathcal{G}_{\mathcal{W}_{i}}
and
A_{i}
is an abelian scheme over
\mathcal{W}_{i}
. Then this induces an extension:
0\to T_{\mathrm{for}}\to\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{for}}\to A_{\mathrm{for}}\to 0
over
\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{for}}
. Since there is a cubical ample invertible sheaf over
\mathcal{G}
, the extension of
\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{for}}
over
\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{for}}
algebraizes uniquely to a so-called
Raynaud extension
:
0\to T\xrightarrow{i}\mathcal{G}^{\natural}\xrightarrow{\pi}A\to 0
over
\mathcal{W}
. Similarly, there is a canonical Raynaud extension for
\mathcal{G}^{\vee}
:
0\to T^{\vee}\xrightarrow{i^{\vee}}\mathcal{G}^{\vee,\natural}\xrightarrow{\pi^{\vee}}A^{\vee}\to 0
. See
[
44
, Sec. 3.3.3 and 3.4.4]
. Thus,
\lambda
canonically defines two morphisms
\phi^{\vee}:T\to T^{\vee}
and
\lambda_{A}:A\to A^{\vee}
, where the former one determines a dual morphism
\phi:\underline{Y}\to\underline{X}
. Then it suffices to construct
\tau_{\eta}
.

(2)

Fix an auxiliary ample cubical invertible sheaf
\mathcal{L}
over
\mathcal{G}
which induces some polarization
\lambda^{\prime}
(possibly different from
\lambda
).
Then
\mathcal{L}
induces an ample cubical invertible sheaf
\mathcal{L}_{\mathrm{for}}
over the formal completion
\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{for}}
and algebraizes to an ample cubical invertible sheaf
\mathcal{L}^{\natural}
over
\mathcal{G}^{\natural}
. Replacing
\mathcal{W}
with a finite étale cover, we assume that
T
and
T^{\vee}
split with character groups
\mathbf{X}^{*}(T)=X
and
\mathbf{X}^{*}(T^{\vee})=Y
.
Then we can fix a cubical trivialization
\mathbf{tri}:i^{*}\mathcal{L}^{\natural}\cong\mathcal{O}_{T}
and find an ample invertible sheaf
\mathcal{M}
over
A
such that
\mathcal{L}^{\natural}\cong\pi^{*}\mathcal{M}
under this trivialization; moreover, we can write
\pi_{*}\mathcal{L}^{\natural}
as a direct sum
\pi_{*}\mathcal{L}^{\natural}\cong\bigoplus_{\chi\in X}\mathcal{M}_{\chi}
, where
\mathcal{M}_{\chi}:=\mathcal{M}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{A}}\mathcal{O}_{\chi}
, and
\mathcal{O}_{\chi}
is the invertible sheaf corresponding to the
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-torsor defined by the pushout of
\mathcal{G}^{\natural}
along
-\chi
. By
[
44
, Prop. 3.1.5.1]
, we see that
\mathcal{O}_{\chi}\cong(\mathrm{Id}_{A}\times c(\chi))^{*}\mathcal{P}_{A}
. Denote by
p_{\chi}:\pi_{*}\mathcal{L}^{\natural}\to\mathcal{M}_{\chi}
the projection to the direct factor
\mathcal{M}_{\chi}
of
\pi_{*}\mathcal{L}^{\natural}
canonically determined by the fixed trivialization
\mathbf{tri}
; this projection determines a morphism
\sigma_{\chi}:\Gamma(\mathcal{G}_{\eta},\mathcal{L}_{\eta})\hookrightarrow\Gamma(\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{for},\eta},\mathcal{L}_{\mathrm{for},\eta})\cong\widehat{\bigoplus}_{\chi\in X}\Gamma(A_{\eta},\mathcal{M}_{\chi,\eta})\to\Gamma(A_{\eta},\mathcal{M}_{\chi,\eta})
. We then need to show a technical fact that
\sigma_{\chi}\neq 0
for all
\chi\in X
using addition formulas for theta representations (see
[
44
, Sec. 4.3.3]
).

(3)

There is an isomorphism
T^{*}_{c^{\vee}(y)}\mathcal{M}_{\chi}\cong\mathcal{M}_{\chi+\phi(y)}\otimes\mathcal{M}_{\chi}(c^{\vee}(y)).
Let
K(\mathcal{M}_{\chi,\eta})
be the kernel of
\lambda_{\mathcal{M}_{\chi,\eta}}
, the polarization determined by
\mathcal{M}_{\chi,\eta}
. Then there is a group
\mathcal{G}(\mathcal{M}_{\chi,\eta})
, which is an extension of
K(\mathcal{M}_{\chi,\eta})
by
\mathbb{G}_{m,\eta}
, defined by the Weil pairing over
K(\mathcal{M}_{\chi,\eta})\times K(\mathcal{M}_{\chi,\eta})
(see
[
44
, Sec. 3.2.4]
). For any
y\in Y
, there is a canonical isomorphism
\mathcal{G}(\mathcal{M}_{\chi,\eta})\cong\mathcal{G}(\mathcal{M}_{\chi+\phi(y),\eta})
depending only on the trivialization
\mathbf{tri}
(see
[
44
, Lem. 4.3.2.4]
).
For any
\overline{\chi}\in X/\phi(Y)
, there is a weight-
\overline{\chi}
space
\Gamma(\mathcal{G}_{\eta},\mathcal{L}_{\eta})_{\overline{\chi}}
, whose choice depends only on
\mathbf{tri}
, such that
\Gamma(\mathcal{G}_{\eta},\mathcal{L}_{\eta})\cong\bigoplus_{\overline{\chi}\in X/\phi(Y)}\Gamma(\mathcal{G}_{\eta},\mathcal{L}_{\eta})_{\overline{\chi}}
and such that
\sigma_{\chi}
factors as a sequence
\sigma_{\chi}:\Gamma(\mathcal{G}_{\eta},\mathcal{L}_{\eta})\to\Gamma(\mathcal{G}_{\eta},\mathcal{L}_{\eta})_{\overline{\chi}}\xrightarrow{\overline{\sigma}_{\chi}}\Gamma(A_{\eta},\mathcal{M}_{\chi,\eta})
, where the first map is the natural projection and the second map
\overline{\sigma}_{\chi}
is
\mathcal{G}(\mathcal{M}_{\chi,\eta})
-equivariant. See
[
44
, Lem. 4.3.2.3 and Lem. 4.3.2.5]
.
In fact, it can be shown that
\overline{\sigma}_{\chi}
is an isomorphism of irreducible representations of
\mathcal{G}(\mathcal{M}_{\chi,\eta})
(see
[
44
, Lem. 4.3.2.9]
).
Thus, by comparing
T^{*}_{c^{\vee}(y)}\circ\overline{\sigma}_{\chi}:\Gamma(\mathcal{G}_{\eta},\mathcal{L}_{\eta})_{\overline{\chi}}\to\Gamma(A_{\eta},\mathcal{M}_{\chi+\phi(y),\eta})\otimes\mathcal{M}_{\chi}(c^{\vee}(y))_{\eta}
and
\overline{\sigma}_{\chi+\phi(y)}:\Gamma(\mathcal{G}_{\eta},\mathcal{L}_{\eta})_{\overline{\chi}}\to\Gamma(A_{\eta},\mathcal{M}_{\chi+\phi(y),\eta})
as isomorphisms of
nonzero
and
irreducible
representations of
\mathcal{G}(\mathcal{M}_{\chi,\eta})
, there is a well-defined function
\bm{\psi}(-,-)\in\mathcal{M}_{\chi}(c^{\vee}(y))_{\eta}^{\otimes-1}
such that
\bm{\psi}(\chi,y)(T^{*}_{c^{\vee}(y)}\circ\overline{\sigma}_{\chi})=\overline{\sigma}_{\chi+\phi(y)}
(see
[
44
, Prop. 4.3.2.10]
).
Let
\bm{\psi}(y):=\bm{\psi}(0,y)
and let
\tau_{\eta}(\chi,y)=\bm{\psi}(y)^{-1}\bm{\psi}(\chi,y)
. Then it can be shown that
\tau_{\eta}
is symmetric with repsect to
\phi
and satisfies the positivity conditions (see
[
44
, Prop. 4.3.1.9]
).

(4)

One can show that the definition of
\tau_{\eta}
does not depend on auxiliary choices, that is, it does not depend on the choice of
\mathcal{L}
and the trivialization
\mathbf{tri}
. Eventually, it can be shown that
\tau_{\eta}
does not depend on
\lambda^{\prime}
. See
[
44
, Cor. 4.3.4.4 and Prop. 4.5.5.1]
.

Theorem 2.34

(
[
21
, III. Cor. 7.2]
; see also
[
44
, Thm. 4.4.16]
)
.

The functor

\mathbf{F}_{\mathrm{pol}}(R,I):\mathbf{DEG}_{\mathrm{pol}}(R,I)\longrightarrow\mathbf{DD}_{\mathrm{pol}}(R,I)

sending
(\mathcal{G},\lambda)
to the pair
(\mathcal{Q}_{\eta},\bm{\lambda}_{\eta})
constructed as above is an equivalence of categories.

Remark 2.35
.

The quasi-inverse of the functor
\mathbf{F}_{\mathrm{pol}}(R,I)
is the so-called “Mumford’s construction”
\mathbf{M}_{\mathrm{pol}}(R,I)
. Note that the independence of
\tau_{\eta}
on the choice of
\lambda
is actually proved after the construction of
\mathbf{M}_{\mathrm{pol}}(R,I)
; see
[
44
, Sec. 4.5.5]
.

2.3.3.

Fix a pair
(R,I)
as before.
Let us briefly recall the construction of the quasi-inverse of the functor
\mathbf{F}_{\mathrm{pol}}(R,I)
, the so-called
Mumford’s construction

\mathbf{M}_{\mathrm{pol}}(R,I)
; see
[
59
]
,
[
21
, Ch. III]
and
[
44
, Sec. 4.5]
.

Let
\mathbf{DEG}(R,I)
(resp.
\mathbf{DD}(R,I)
) be the category whose objects are
\mathcal{G}
(resp.
\mathcal{Q}_{\eta}
) that can be extended to an object
(\mathcal{G},\lambda)\in\ob\mathbf{DEG}_{\mathrm{pol}}(R,I)
(resp.
(\mathcal{Q}_{\eta},\bm{\lambda}_{\eta})\in\ob\mathbf{DD}_{\mathrm{pol}}(R,I)
). The morphisms in
\mathbf{DEG}(R,I)
(resp.
\mathbf{DD}(R,I)
) are the morphisms between semi-abelian schemes (resp.
1
-motives) that can be extended to
\mor(\mathbf{DEG}(R,I))
(resp.
\mor(\mathbf{DD}(R,I))
).
If we forget polarizations, it turns out that there is also a functor
\mathbf{F}(R,I):\mathbf{DEG}(R,I)\to\mathbf{DD}(R,I)
that is compatible with
\mathbf{F}_{\mathrm{pol}}
, see below.

Construction 2.36

(
[
59
]
,
[
21
, Ch. III]
and
[
44
, Sec. 4.5]
)
.

Fix some
(\mathcal{Q}_{\eta},\bm{\lambda}_{\eta})\in\ob\mathbf{DD}_{\mathrm{pol}}(R,I)
. The so-called “Mumford’s construction” is established in the following steps:

(1)

Suppose that we are given an ample invertible sheaf
\mathcal{M}
over
A
. Let
\mathcal{L}^{\natural}:=\pi^{*}\mathcal{M}
, the pullback of
\mathcal{M}
via
\pi:\mathcal{G}^{\natural}\to A
. Suppose that there is a cubical trivialization
\bm{\psi}:\underline{Y}\to\mathcal{L}^{\natural,\otimes-1}
and an injective
\phi:\underline{Y}\to\underline{X}
with finite cokernel. Furthermore, we can assume that
\underline{Y}=Y
and
\underline{X}=X
, i.e.,
\underline{Y}
and
\underline{X}
split. The idea is to construct some “compactification”
(\mathscr{P}^{\natural},\mathcal{L}^{\natural})
.

(2)

The first fact is that,
under some technical condition
on
(\mathcal{L}^{\natural},\bm{\psi},\phi)
(see
[
21
, Ch. III, p. 62]
, there is a
relatively complete model

\mathscr{P}^{\natural}
. To be precise,
\mathscr{P}^{\natural}
is an integral and locally of finite type scheme over
A
, which contains
\mathcal{G}^{\natural}
as an open dense subscheme and satisfies the following properties (see
[
21
, Ch. III, Def. 3.1]
):

(a)

The invertible sheaf
\mathcal{L}^{\natural}
extends to an invertible sheaf over
\mathscr{P}^{\natural}
, which we also denote by
\mathcal{L}^{\natural}
. Moreover,
\mathcal{L}^{\natural}|_{V}
is ample over any quasi-compact open subscheme
V
of
\mathscr{P}^{\natural}
.

(b)

The translation action
T:\mathcal{G}^{\natural}\times\mathcal{G}^{\natural}\to\mathcal{G}^{\natural}
extends to an action of
\mathcal{G}^{\natural}
on
\mathscr{P}^{\natural}
, i.e., a
\mathcal{G}^{\natural}
-action
T:\mathcal{G}^{\natural}\times\mathscr{P}^{\natural}\to\mathscr{P}^{\natural}
. Denote by
T_{g}
the translation action of
g\in\mathcal{G}^{\natural}(S^{\prime})
for any
S^{\prime}
over
\mathcal{W}
. Moreover, the translation action on
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{G}^{\natural}}
extends to an action
T^{*}_{g}\mathcal{N}\to\mathcal{N}
on
\mathcal{N}:=\mathcal{L}^{\natural}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathscr{P}^{\natural}}}\pi^{*}\mathcal{M}^{\otimes-1}
.

(c)

We assume that
\underline{Y}=Y
splits. Recall that there is a
Y
-action
(\iota,\bm{\psi})
on
(\mathcal{G}^{\natural}_{\eta},\mathcal{L}^{\natural}_{\eta})
. Then there is a
Y
-action on
(\mathscr{P}^{\natural},\mathcal{L}^{\natural})
extending
(\iota,\bm{\psi})
; we denote by
(S,\widetilde{S})
this extended action, and by
(S_{y},\widetilde{S}_{y})
the evaluation of this action at any point
y\in Y
.

(d)

There is a
\mathcal{G}^{\natural}
-invariant open subscheme
U
of
\mathscr{P}^{\natural}
, such that
\mathscr{P}^{\natural}=\bigcup\limits_{y\in Y}S_{y}(U)
.

(e)

\mathscr{P}^{\natural}
satisfies the
completeness condition
as in
[
21
, Ch. III, 3, p.61]
.

Note that not all
(\mathcal{L}^{\natural},\bm{\psi},\phi)
satisfy the
technical condition
on
[
21
, Ch. III, p. 62]
, but for any
\mathcal{Q}_{\eta}
, one can find some
(\mathcal{L}^{\natural},\bm{\psi},\phi)
satisfying this condition.

(3)

Denote
\mathscr{P}^{\natural}_{i}:=\mathscr{P}^{\natural}\times_{\mathcal{W}}\mathcal{W}_{i}
. Every irreducible component of
\mathscr{P}^{\natural}_{i}
is proper. Moreover,
S_{y}
acts on each
\mathscr{P}_{i}^{\natural}
, and the quotient fpqc sheaf
\mathscr{P}_{i}:=\mathscr{P}_{i}^{\natural}/Y
is representable by a projective scheme. The morphism
\mathscr{P}_{i}^{\natural}\to\mathscr{P}_{i}
is étale and surjective. The invertible sheaf
\mathcal{L}^{\natural}_{i}:=\mathcal{L}^{\natural}\otimes_{R}R_{i}
descends to an ample sheaf
\mathcal{L}_{i}
. For different
i
, such pairs
(\mathscr{P}_{i},\mathcal{L}_{i})
are compatible, and therefore there is a pair
(\mathscr{P}_{\mathrm{for}},\mathcal{L}_{\mathrm{for}})
consisting of the formal completion
\mathscr{P}_{\mathrm{for}}
of
\{\mathscr{P}_{i}\}
and an ample sheaf
\mathcal{L}_{\mathrm{for}}
on it. This pair algebraizes to a pair
(\mathscr{P},\mathcal{L})
of projective scheme
\mathscr{P}
with an ample invertible sheaf
\mathcal{L}
on it.

(4)

Let
\mathcal{G}^{\natural,*}:=\bigcup\limits_{y\in Y}S_{y}(\mathcal{G}^{\natural})\subset\mathscr{P}^{\natural}
. Then
\mathcal{G}^{\natural,*}
is an open subscheme of
\mathscr{P}^{\natural}
. Denote
\mathcal{G}_{i}:=\mathcal{G}^{\natural,*}\times_{\mathcal{W}}\mathcal{W}_{i}
. Consider the complement
C^{\natural}:=(\mathscr{P}^{\natural}-\mathcal{G}^{\natural,*})_{\mathrm{red}}
. Note that
C_{i}
, the quotient by
Y
of the pullback of
C^{\natural}
to
\mathcal{W}_{i}
, are reduced closed subschemes of
\mathscr{P}_{i}
. We can form a
C_{\mathrm{for}}
over
\mathcal{W}
whose pullback to
\mathcal{W}_{i}
is
C_{i}
, and this
C_{\mathrm{for}}
algebraizes to a closed subscheme
C
of
\mathscr{P}
. Let
\mathcal{G}:=\mathscr{P}-C
.
Then it can be shown that
\mathcal{G}
is a semi-abelian scheme, with
\mathcal{G}_{\eta}
an abelian scheme, and with
\mathcal{L}:=\mathcal{L}|_{\mathcal{G}}
an ample invertible sheaf. See
[
44
, Cor. 4.5.3.9 and Cor. 4.5.3.13]
.

(5)

It can be shown that
\mathcal{G}
does not depend on the auxiliary choice of
(\mathcal{L}^{\natural},\bm{\psi},\phi)
and
\mathscr{P}^{\natural}
. Thus, one can form a functor
\mathbf{M}(R,I):\mathbf{DD}(R,I)\to\mathbf{DEG}(R,I)
, which is an equivalence of category. The quasi-inverse of it is denoted by
\mathbf{F}(R,I)
. Moreover, we have
\mathbf{M}(R,I)(\mathcal{Q}_{\eta}^{\vee})=\mathcal{G}^{\vee}
. Thus, there is a
\lambda:\mathcal{G}\to\mathcal{G}^{\vee}\in\mor(\mathbf{DEG}(R,I))
that is defined by applying
\mathbf{M}(R,I)
to
\bm{\lambda}_{\eta}
. In fact, it can be shown that
\lambda
is a polarization, so we can form the functor
\mathbf{M}_{\mathrm{pol}}:\mathbf{DD}_{\mathrm{pol}}(R,I)\to\mathbf{F}_{\mathrm{pol}}(R,I)
;
\mathbf{M}_{\mathrm{pol}}(R,I)
is the quasi-inverse of
\mathbf{F}_{\mathrm{pol}}
.

Let us also recall the construction of relative complete models for the graph of a morphism
f:\mathcal{Q}_{\eta,1}\to\mathcal{Q}_{\eta,2}
in
\mathbf{DD}(R,I)
. See
[
59
, Thm. 4.6]
,
[
21
, Ch. III, Thm. 5.5]
and
[
44
, Thm. 4.5.3.6]
.

Choose
(\mathcal{M}_{i},\mathcal{L}^{\natural}_{i},\bm{\psi}_{i},\phi_{i})
satisfying the condition on
[
21
, Ch. III, p.63]
as in Construction
2.36
for
\mathcal{Q}_{\eta,1}
and
\mathcal{Q}_{\eta,2}
, respectively. Then there are two relative complete models
\mathscr{P}^{\natural}_{1}
and
\mathscr{P}^{\natural}_{2}
for
\mathcal{Q}_{\eta,1}
and
\mathcal{Q}_{\eta,2}
, respectively.

f^{sab}
extends to a morphism
f^{sab}:\mathcal{G}^{\natural}_{1}\to\mathcal{G}^{\natural}_{2}
over
\mathcal{W}
. If we let
W_{1}^{\natural}
be the schematic closure of the graph
H^{\natural}
of
f^{sab}
in
\mathscr{P}_{1}^{\natural}\times_{\mathcal{W}}\mathscr{P}_{2}^{\natural}
.
Then
W_{\mathrm{for}}^{\natural}:=\bigcup\limits_{(y_{1},y_{2})\in Y_{1}\times Y_{2}}S_{(y_{1},y_{2})}W_{1,\mathrm{for}}^{\natural}
is a locally finite union.
Then it can be shown that
W_{\mathrm{for}}:=W_{\mathrm{for}}^{\natural}/Y_{1}\times Y_{2}
is a formal subscheme of
P_{1,\mathrm{for}}\times_{\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{for}}}P_{2,\mathrm{for}}
; moreover,
W_{\mathrm{for}}
algebraizes to a unique
W\subset P_{1}\times_{\mathcal{W}}P_{2}
. Then it can be shown that
H=W\cap(\mathcal{G}_{1}\times_{\mathcal{W}}\mathcal{G}_{2})
is the graph of a uniquely determined
g:G_{1}\to G_{2}
, and we let
g=\mathbf{M}(f)
.

2.3.4.

The
n
-torsion points of
\mathcal{G}_{\eta}
and
\mathcal{Q}_{\eta}
are isomorphic. More precisely, they are canonically isomorphic under
\mathbf{M}
.

Lemma 2.37
.

Suppose that
n
is prime to
p
and that
\mathbf{M}(\mathcal{Q}_{\eta})=\mathcal{G}
.
Then there is a canonical isomorphism
\mathcal{Q}_{\eta}[n]\cong\mathcal{G}_{\eta}[n]
induced by
\mathbf{M}
.

Proof.

We can see this in the proof of
[
59
, Thm. 4.10]
,
[
21
, Thm. 5.9]
and
[
44
, Thm. 4.5.3.10]
. Let us repeat this for the convenience of the reader.

By étale descent, we assume that the locally constant group part of all
1
-motives we are working on are constant, i.e.,
\underline{X}=X
and
\underline{Y}=Y
.
Let
\mathscr{P}^{\natural}
be a relative complete model for
\mathcal{Q}_{\eta}
.
Let
{}^{(n)}{Z}^{\natural}_{\eta}
be
Y\times_{\iota,\mathcal{G}^{\natural}_{\eta},[n]}\mathcal{G}^{\natural}_{\eta}
. Let
{}^{(n)}{Z}^{\natural}_{y,\eta}
be
y\times_{\iota,\mathcal{G}^{\natural}_{\eta},[n]}\mathcal{G}^{\natural}_{\eta}
. We see that
{}^{(n)}{Z}_{y,\eta}^{\natural}\cong{}^{(n)}{Z}_{y+nz,\eta}^{\natural}
for any
z\in Y
. Then, if we choose any set of representatives of
Y/nY
in
Y
,
\coprod_{y\in Y/nY}{}^{(n)}{Z}^{\natural}_{y,\eta}
is isomorphic to
\mathcal{Q}_{\eta}[n]
. Let
\sigma_{y}:\mathcal{W}\to\mathcal{G}^{\natural,*}
be the unique section that
\sigma_{y}(\eta)=\iota(y)
.
Let
{}^{(n)}{Z}_{y}^{\natural}:=\mathcal{W}\times_{\sigma_{y},\mathcal{G}^{\natural,*},[n]}\mathcal{G}^{\natural,*}
. Since
\mathcal{G}^{\natural,*}_{\eta}=\bigcup_{y}\iota(y)(\mathcal{G}^{\natural}_{\eta})=\mathcal{G}^{\natural}_{\eta}
,
{}^{(n)}{Z}^{\natural}_{y,\eta}
is dense in
{}^{(n)}{Z}^{\natural}_{y}
.

Denote by
{}^{(n)}{W}^{\natural}_{1}
the schematic closure of the graph of
[n]
in
\mathscr{P}^{\natural}\times\mathscr{P}^{\natural}
. Recall that
\iota
extends to an action
S
over
\mathscr{P}^{\natural}
. Denote
S_{(a,b)}:=S_{a}\times S_{b}
. Denote
{}^{(n)}{W}^{\natural}:=\bigcup\limits_{y\in Y}S_{(0,y)}{}^{(n)}{W}_{1}^{\natural}
.

Then

\bigcup\limits_{y\in Y}(\overline{{}^{(n)}{Z}^{\natural}_{y}})_{\mathrm{for}}={}^{(n)}{W}^{\natural}_{\mathrm{for}}\cap(\mathscr{P}_{\mathrm{for}}^{\natural}\times_{\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{for}}}\sigma_{0}\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{for}}),

and both the left-hand side and the right-hand side are locally finite unions.

Let
(\overline{{}^{(n)}{Z}})_{\mathrm{for}}:=\bigcup\limits_{y\in Y}(\overline{{}^{(n)}{Z}^{\natural}_{y}})_{\mathrm{for}}/Y={}^{(n)}{W}_{\mathrm{for}}\cap(\mathscr{P}_{\mathrm{for}}\times_{\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{for}}}\sigma_{0}\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{for}}),
where
{}^{(n)}{W}_{\mathrm{for}}:={}^{(n)}{W}_{\mathrm{for}}^{\natural}/Y\times Y
. Then

(\overline{{}^{(n)}{Z}})_{\mathrm{for}}
algebraizes to a closed subscheme
\overline{{}^{(n)}{Z}}
of
P
, and

\overline{{}^{(n)}{Z}}={}^{(n)}{W}\cap(P\times\sigma_{0}\mathcal{W}).

On the other hand, for any finite subset
Y_{0}\subset Y
, there is a morphism

\overline{q}_{\mathrm{for}}:\bigcup\limits_{y\in Y_{0}}(\overline{{}^{(n)}{Z}^{\natural}_{y}})_{\mathrm{for}}\longrightarrow(\overline{{}^{(n)}{Z}})_{\mathrm{for}}.

Since
\overline{q}_{\mathrm{for}}
is a morphism between formal completions of proper schemes over
\mathcal{W}
,
\overline{q}_{\mathrm{for}}
algebraizes to a morphism
\overline{q}
.
Since
\overline{q}_{\mathrm{for}}^{-1}(\overline{{}^{(n)}{Z}}_{\mathrm{for}}\cap\mathscr{P}_{\mathrm{for}}\times C_{\mathrm{for}})\subset\mathscr{P}^{\natural}_{\mathrm{for}}\times C^{\natural}_{\mathrm{for}}
,

\overline{q}^{-1}(\overline{{}^{(n)}{Z}}\cap\mathscr{P}\times C)\subset\mathscr{P}^{\natural}\times C^{\natural}
.
Then
\overline{q}
restricts to

q:\bigcup\limits_{y\in Y_{0}}({}^{(n)}{Z}^{\natural}_{y})\longrightarrow({}^{(n)}{Z}).

Choose
Y_{0}
to be any set of representatives of
Y/nY
in
Y
. Then it turns out that
q
is étale, surjective and of degree one; see the proof of
loc. cit
. In particular,
q
is an isomorphism. Hence,
q_{\eta}
is the desired isomorphism induced by Mumford’s construction.
∎

2.3.5.

Next, we recall the definition of level structures of degeneration data, which can be used to describe the boundary mixed Shimura varieties of
\mathcal{S}_{K^{\ddagger}}
. Let

\mathbb{Z}

Remark 2.40
.

Before presenting the proof, we make the following remarks:

(1)

One might not deduce this equivalence
verbatim
from the proof of
[
44
, Thm. 5.2.3.14 and Cor. 5.2.3.15]
since the pair
(\mathcal{Q},\bm{\lambda})
might not come from
\mathbf{F}_{\mathrm{pol}}(R,I)
, in particular, we are not assuming positive conditions for
\tau
imposed in
\mathbf{DD}_{\mathrm{pol}}(R,I)
. Nevertheless, we will closely follow the idea and the arguments of
loc. cit.
in the proof below.

(2)

Note that Part
3
is helpful in the construction and the
detailed
description of boundary charts (see
[
44
, Ch. 6]
). For our purpose, it is more convenient for us to directly use Part
1
in order to study the twisting action on the family of
1
-motives with additional structures in the next section.

(3)

One can change the sign conventions in the definition of Weil pairings (see Definition
2.20
) to get a possibly different sign in the pairings of the proposition above. We choose a convention that makes our results compatible with
[
44
]
.

We explain the proof of Proposition
2.39
in steps.

Proof of Proposition
2.39
, part
1
\Longleftrightarrow
part
2
.

Part
1
\implies
part
2
can be directly deduced from
[
44
, Lem. 5.2.2.14]
. Since
G^{\ddagger}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
has a
right
action on
(Z^{(g^{{\ddagger}})},\Phi^{(g^{{\ddagger}})})
whose stabilizer is
g^{{\ddagger},-1}P_{\Phi^{\ddagger}}(\mathbb{A}_{f})g^{{\ddagger}}
, part
2
\implies
part
1
also follows from
loc. cit.
; while applying
loc. cit.
, one can replace
\overline{\eta}
there with a general base
S
and replace
(G,\lambda)
there with
(\mathcal{Q},\bm{\lambda})
.

\square

Next, we show part
2
\Longleftrightarrow
part
3
.

Proof of Proposition
2.39
, except that
2(.1)
\Longleftrightarrow
3(.5)
.

First, a splitting
\hat{\varsigma}_{12}:\gr^{W_{T^{\square}\mathcal{Q}}}_{-1}\oplus\gr^{W_{T^{\square}\mathcal{Q}}}_{-2}\xrightarrow{\sim}T^{\square}\mathcal{G}^{\natural}
determines and is determined by an inverse system of splittings
\varsigma_{12,n}:\gr^{W_{T^{\square}\mathcal{Q}}}_{-1}[n]\oplus\gr^{W_{T^{\square}\mathcal{Q}}}_{-2}[n]\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathcal{G}^{\natural}[n]
, for
p\nmid n
if
\square=\{p\}
.

Write
\mathcal{G}^{\natural}
as the extension
1\rightarrow T\xrightarrow{i}\mathcal{G}^{\natural}\xrightarrow{\pi}A\rightarrow 1
. For any positive integer
n
, the splitting
\varsigma_{12,n}:\gr^{W_{T^{\square}\mathcal{Q}}}_{-1}[n]\oplus\gr^{W_{T^{\square}\mathcal{Q}}}_{-2}[n]\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathcal{G}^{\natural}[n]
determines and is determined by a projection
p_{T}:\mathcal{G}^{\natural}[n]\to T[n]
such that
p_{T}\circ i=\mathrm{Id}
; this is equivalent to an embedding
\overline{c}_{n}:\underline{X}/n\underline{X}\to\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}_{S-gp}(\mathcal{G}^{\natural}[n],\mathbb{G}_{m})\cong\mathcal{Q}^{\vee,\circ}[n]
such that
\overline{p}_{X}\circ\overline{c}_{n}=\mathrm{Id}
, after taking
\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}_{S-gp}(-,\mathbb{G}_{m})
. In the last sentence,
\mathcal{Q}^{\vee,\circ}:=\mathcal{Q}^{\vee}/T^{\vee}=[\underline{X}\xrightarrow{\ c\ }A^{\vee}]
, and
\overline{p}_{X}
is a quotient of the natural projection to the first factor
\mathcal{Q}^{\vee,\circ}[n]\xrightarrow{p_{1}}\underline{X}\xrightarrow{\ }\underline{X}/n\underline{X}
.

Then we can see that such a splitting
\overline{c}_{n}
determines and is determined by a lifting
c_{n}:\frac{1}{n}\underline{X}\to A^{\vee}
of
c
such that the following diagram commutes:

(2.14)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\frac{1}{n}\underline{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A^{\vee}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A^{\vee},}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c_{n}}

where the natural isomorphism
\underline{X}\cong\frac{1}{n}\underline{X}
is induced by “dividing by

” of coefficients
\mathbb{Z}\cong\mathbb{Z}\cdot\frac{1}{n}
.

In fact, this follows from the definition of
n
-torsion subgroup of
1
-motives. We can assume that
\underline{X}=X
. Fix any

-scheme

. If there is a splitting

, for any
\overline{\chi}\in X/nX(S^{\prime})
, we can therefore find a pair
(\chi,a^{\vee})
, lifting
\overline{c}_{n}(\overline{\chi})
, such that
\chi\in X(S^{\prime})
lifting
\overline{\chi}
,
a^{\vee}\in A^{\vee}(S^{\prime})
, and

. Define

.
For any other choice of the pair

lifting
\overline{c}_{n}(\overline{\chi})
,

. Then there is a well-defined homomorphism

lifting

.
Conversely, assume that there is a lifting

. Choose any set of representatives

of
\frac{1}{n}X/X
in

.
Then define

by

, which maps any
\overline{\chi}\in X/nX
represented by
\chi
to the class represented by
(\chi,c_{n}(\frac{1}{n}\chi))
in
\mathcal{Q}^{\vee,\circ}[n]
.

A splitting
\hat{\varsigma}:\bigoplus\limits_{i=0}^{2}\mathrm{Gr}^{W_{T^{\square}\mathcal{Q}}}_{-i}\xrightarrow{\sim}T^{\square}\mathcal{Q}
determines and is determined by
\hat{\varsigma}_{12}
and the restriction
\hat{\varsigma}_{0}:=\hat{\varsigma}|_{\mathrm{Gr}^{W_{T^{\square}\mathcal{Q}}}_{0}}
. And
\hat{\varsigma}_{0}
is an inverse limit, over all

such that
\square\nmid n
, of splittings
\varsigma_{0,n}:\underline{Y}/n\underline{Y}\to\mathcal{Q}[n]
of the exact sequence

. Then, by the same argument as in the third and the fourth paragraph of this proof, a splitting
\varsigma_{0,n}
determines and is determined by liftings
\iota_{n}:\frac{1}{n}\underline{Y}\to\mathcal{G}^{\natural}
of
\iota
such that the following diagram commutes:

(2.15)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{Y}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\frac{1}{n}\underline{Y}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{G}^{\natural}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{Y}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{G}^{\natural}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota}

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces[n]}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces[n]}

Then
c_{n}^{\vee}:=\pi\circ\iota_{n}
is a lifting of
c^{\vee}
, which corresponds to the splitting
\pi\circ\varsigma_{0,n}:\underline{Y}/n\underline{Y}\to\mathcal{Q}[n]/T[n]=\mathcal{Q}^{\circ}[n]
of the exact sequence
0\to A[n]\to\mathcal{Q}^{\circ}[n]\to\underline{Y}/n\underline{Y}\to 0
.

Let
\widetilde{c}:=\varprojlim_{n,\square\nmid n}c_{n}
,
\widetilde{c}^{\vee}:=\varprojlim_{n,\square\nmid n}c_{n}^{\vee}
and
\widetilde{\iota}:=\varprojlim_{n,\square\nmid n}\iota_{n}
;
\widetilde{\iota}
corresponds to a trivialization
\widetilde{\tau}:\mathbf{1}_{(\underline{Y}\otimes\mathbb{Z}_{(\square)})\times\underline{X}}\to(\widetilde{c}^{\vee}\times c)^{*}\mathcal{P}_{A}^{\otimes-1}
of biextensions.
Then we have shown that a splitting
\hat{\varsigma}
is equivalent to a tuple of liftings
(\widetilde{c},\widetilde{c}^{\vee},\widetilde{\iota})
which lifts the tuple
(c,c^{\vee},\iota)
; this tuple of liftings is further equivalent to a tuple
(\widetilde{c},\widetilde{c}^{\vee},\widetilde{\tau})
.

\square

Note that we will complete the proof of Proposition
2.39
if we show
2(.1)
\Longleftrightarrow
3(.5)
.

Lemma 2.41
.

The image of
\varsigma_{n}(A[n])\subset\mathcal{G}^{\natural}[n]
lifts to
n
-torsion points in
\mathcal{G}^{\natural,\prime}
.

Proof.

The homomorphism
\overline{c}_{n}
determines a homomorphism
\overline{\pi}^{\vee}:\mathcal{Q}^{\vee,\circ}[n]\to A^{\vee}[n]
: Indeed, for any
[(\chi,a)]\in\mathcal{Q}^{\vee,\circ}[n]
such that
\chi\in X
and
a\in A^{\vee}(S^{\prime})
,
a-\overline{c}_{n}(\overline{\chi})\in A^{\vee}(S^{\prime})
. Then
\overline{\pi}^{\vee}:[(\chi,a)]\mapsto a-\overline{c}_{n}(\overline{\chi})
is a well-defined homomorphism. Since
\overline{c}_{n}
is a splitting of
0\to A^{\vee}[n]\to\mathcal{Q}^{\vee,\circ}[n]\to\underline{X}/n\underline{X}\to 0
,
\ker\overline{\pi}^{\vee}=\im\overline{c}_{n}
and
\overline{\pi}^{\vee}|_{A^{\vee}[n]}=\mathrm{Id}_{A^{\vee}[n]}
.
On the other hand, from the second step of the proof of Proposition
2.39
, the image of
\overline{c}_{n}
in
\mathcal{Q}^{\vee,\circ}[n]
is
\im(n,c_{n})/\im(n,c)
. So the image of
\overline{\pi}^{\vee}:\mathcal{Q}^{\vee,\circ}[n]\to A^{\vee}[n]
is isomorphic to the image of the natural homomorphism
\mathcal{Q}^{\vee,\circ}[n]\to\mathcal{R}^{\vee,\circ}[n]
, where
\mathcal{R}^{\vee,\circ}:=[\frac{1}{n}\underline{X}\xrightarrow{c_{n}}A^{\vee}]
. Then, by Cartier duality, we find that
\varsigma_{n}(A[n])\subset[n]_{T}(\mathcal{G}^{\natural,\prime}[n])
.
∎

\mathcal{P}
” and “
\mathcal{O}
” denote biextensions or bundles, but not invertible sheaves.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{O}_{S^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{O}_{S^{\prime}}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{can}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{can}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces[n]}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{can}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{can}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{can}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varrho_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varrho_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varrho_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{e}^{\bm{\lambda}}(\varsigma_{n}(t),\varsigma_{n}(\overline{y}))}

Finally, viewing
T
as a subgroup of
\mathcal{G}^{\natural,\prime}
, we conclude that
\mathrm{e}^{\bm{\lambda}}(t,(y,g))
is the difference between the evaluation of
\phi_{n}(y):T\to\mathbb{G}_{m}
at
\widetilde{t}^{n}
and the evaluation of
\phi_{n}(y)
at
\widetilde{t}^{n^{2}}
. By the sign conventions in Lemma
2.19
,
\mathrm{e}^{\bm{\lambda}}(t,(y,g))=\phi^{*}(y)(t)
.

\square

Note that the Cartier dual of

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{Y}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{G}^{\natural}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\frac{1}{n}\underline{Y}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota_{n}}

is a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{G}^{\natural,\wedge}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{G}^{\natural,\vee}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\iota}^{\vee}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota^{\vee}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces n^{\vee}}

Denote by
\mathcal{Q}^{\wedge}:=[\underline{X}\xrightarrow{\widetilde{\iota}^{\vee}}\mathcal{G}^{\natural,\wedge}]
the Cartier dual of
\mathcal{Q}_{n}:=[\frac{1}{n}\underline{Y}\xrightarrow{\iota_{n}}\mathcal{G}^{\natural}]
. The homomorphism

is
T^{\vee}\xrightarrow{x\mapsto x^{n}}T^{\vee}
when restricted to torus parts.

More precisely, we have the following commutative diagram which is similar to (
2.18
):

(2.24)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{G}^{\natural,\wedge}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{Y}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{G}^{\natural,\vee}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\frac{1}{n}\underline{Y}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{G}^{\natural}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\iota}^{\vee}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota^{\vee}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\phi^{c}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces n^{\vee}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\bm{\lambda}}^{c,sab}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota_{n}}

To form the diagram above, we can find a polarization
\bm{\lambda}^{c}:\mathcal{Q}^{\vee}\to\mathcal{Q}
such that
\bm{\lambda}\circ\bm{\lambda}^{c}=[N]_{\mathcal{Q}^{\vee}}
and
\bm{\lambda}^{c}\circ\bm{\lambda}=[N]_{\mathcal{Q}}
for some integer
N>0
; then we denote
\bm{\lambda}^{c}:=(\bm{\lambda}^{c,et},\bm{\lambda}^{c,sab})
and
\bm{\lambda}^{c,et}:=\phi^{c}
.

Proof of Proposition
2.39
,
2(.1)
\Longleftrightarrow

:
\mathbf{d}_{00,n}=\mathrm{e}_{00,n}
.

Suppose that
\varsigma_{n}(\overline{y}_{i})=(y_{i},g_{i})\in\mathcal{Q}[n]
for some
y_{i}\in Y

2.4.
Boundary mixed Shimura varieties of Hodge type, continued

Fix a cusp label representative
\Phi_{0}=(Q,X^{+}_{0},g_{0})
of
(G_{0},X_{0})
. With the conventions in §
2.3.5
, suppose that
\Phi_{0}
is sent to a cusp label representative
\Phi^{\ddagger}
of
(G^{\ddagger},X^{\ddagger})
under
\iota
. Let us describe the moduli interpretation of
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi^{\ddagger}}}(\mathbb{C})
as a family of polarized
1
-motives with level structures.

2.4.1.

Recall that
D_{\Phi_{0}}:=D_{Q,X^{+}_{0}}
is defined to be the
P_{\Phi_{0}}(\mathbb{R})U_{\Phi_{0}}(\mathbb{C})
-orbit of
([x],u_{x}^{Q}\circ h_{\infty})\in\pi_{0}(X_{0})\times\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathbb{C}}(\mathbb{S}_{\mathbb{C}},P_{\Phi_{0},\mathbb{C}})
, for any
x\in X_{0}^{+}
.
Moreover, by Proposition
1.8
,
D_{\Phi_{0}}
is in fact the
P_{\Phi_{0}}(\mathbb{R})U_{\Phi_{0}}(\mathbb{C})
-orbit of
u_{x}^{Q}\circ h_{\infty}\in\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathbb{C}}(\mathbb{S}_{\mathbb{C}},P_{\Phi_{0},\mathbb{C}})
, and the morphism
D_{\Phi_{0}}\to D_{\Phi^{\ddagger}}\subset\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathbb{C}}(\mathbb{S}_{\mathbb{C}},P_{\Phi^{\ddagger},\mathbb{C}})
induced by
\iota
is injective.

For any
x^{\ddagger}\in D_{\Phi^{\ddagger}}
,
x^{\ddagger}
determines a rational mixed Hodge structure
\mathbf{M}_{\mathrm{MH}}(x^{\ddagger}):=(V,F^{\bullet}_{x^{\ddagger}},W_{\bullet}^{x^{\ddagger}})
on the
\mathbb{Q}
-vector space
V
, where
F^{\bullet}_{x^{\ddagger}}
is a descending filtration on
V_{\mathbb{C}}
and where
W_{\bullet}^{x^{\ddagger}}
is an ascending filtration on
V
; by
[
64
, Prop. 1.16(c)]
, the association
x^{\ddagger}\mapsto\mathbf{M}_{\mathrm{MH}}(x^{\ddagger})
is injective.
For any
\mathbb{Z}
-lattice
\mathfrak{V}\subset V
,
\mathbf{M}_{\mathrm{MH}}(x^{\ddagger})
determines an integral mixed Hodge structure
\mathbb{M}_{\mathrm{MH}}(\mathfrak{V},x^{\ddagger}):=(\mathfrak{V},F^{\bullet}_{x^{\ddagger}}|_{\mathfrak{V}},W_{\bullet}^{x^{\ddagger}}|_{\mathfrak{V}})
;
we will abusively write
\mathbb{M}_{\mathrm{MH}}(\mathfrak{V})
or
\mathbb{M}_{\mathrm{MH}}(x^{\ddagger})
if
x^{\ddagger}
or
\mathfrak{V}
is clear in the context; we apply similar shorthand for
\mathbf{M}_{\mathrm{MH}}
. Note that if
V
and
\mathfrak{V}
are fixed, then
\mathbf{M}_{\mathrm{MH}}(x^{\ddagger})
determines and is determined by
\mathbb{M}_{\mathrm{MH}}(x^{\ddagger})
.

On the other hand,
x^{\ddagger}
induces on
V
a mixed Hodge structure of type (see
[
64
, Ex. 4.25]
)

\{(0,0);(-1,0),(0,-1);(-1,-1)\}.

The category of integral mixed Hodge structures of this type is closely related to the category of
1
-motives over
\mathbb{C}
. In fact, Deligne showed that one can geometrize this type of integral mixed Hodge structures.

Theorem 2.42

(
[
18
, 10.1.3]
)
.

For any
1
-motive
\mathcal{Q}=(\underline{Y},\mathcal{G}^{\natural},T,A,\iota,c^{\vee})
over
\mathbb{C}
, there is a canonical finitely generated free
\mathbb{Z}
-module
T_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathcal{Q}
with Hodge filtration
F^{\bullet}
and weight filtration
W_{\bullet}
on it, such that
(T_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathcal{Q},F^{\bullet},W_{\bullet})
is an integral mixed Hodge structure of type
\{(0,0);(-1,0),(0,-1);(-1,-1)\}
, and such that,
\gr^{W}_{-2}T_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathcal{Q}=H_{1}(T,\mathbb{Z})=X^{\vee}\cong\mathbf{X}_{*}(T)
,
\gr^{W}_{-1}T_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathcal{Q}=H_{1}(A,\mathbb{Z})
and
\gr^{W}_{0}T_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathcal{Q}=Y
.

Moreover, the functor
T_{\mathbb{Z}}(-)
, from the category of
1
-motives over
\mathbb{C}
to the category of integral mixed Hodge structures of type
\{(0,0);(-1,0),(0,-1);(-1,-1)\}
whose weight
-1
graded pieces are polarizable, is an equivalence of category.

The module
T_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathcal{Q}
, equipped with its weight filtration and Hodge filtration, is called the
Hodge realization
of
\mathcal{Q}
. There are canonical comparison isomorphisms between the Hodge realization and the Tate modules, respecting weight filtrations and its graded pieces:
T_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathcal{Q}\otimes\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}\cong\widehat{T}\mathcal{Q}
,
T_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathcal{Q}\otimes\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{p}\cong T^{\square}\mathcal{Q}
and
T_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathcal{Q}\otimes\mathbb{Z}_{p}\cong T_{p}\mathcal{Q}
. For this, see
[
18
, 10.1.5-10.1.10]
.

One can also associate with
\mathcal{Q}
a canonical
de Rham realization

H_{\mathrm{dR}}\mathcal{Q}
. There is a canonical exact sequence

(2.26)

0\to F^{0}H_{\mathrm{dR}}\mathcal{Q}\cong\underline{\mathrm{Lie}}_{\mathcal{G}^{\natural,\vee}/\mathbb{C}}^{\vee}\to H_{\mathrm{dR}}\mathcal{Q}\to\underline{\mathrm{Lie}}_{\mathcal{G}^{\natural}/\mathbb{C}}\to 0.

There is also a canonical comparison
H_{\mathrm{dR}}\mathcal{Q}\cong T_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathcal{Q}\otimes\mathbb{C}
.
For this, see
loc. cit.
, and see
[
7
]
.

2.4.2.

Fix any polarized
1
-motive
(\mathcal{Q},\bm{\lambda})
over
\mathbb{C}
. Denote
\mathcal{Q}=(\underline{Y},\mathcal{G}^{\natural},T,A,\iota,c^{\vee})
. Let
\mathfrak{V}=T_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathcal{Q}
. The Hodge realization of Cartier dual
\mathcal{Q}^{\vee}
,
\mathfrak{V}^{\vee}:=T_{\mathbb{Z}}(\mathcal{Q}^{\vee})
, can be naturally identified with
T_{\mathbb{Z}}(\mathcal{Q}^{\vee})=\mathrm{Hom}(\mathfrak{V},\mathbb{Z}(1))
, induced by a canonical pairing
\Phi^{\mathrm{can}}:\mathfrak{V}\times\mathfrak{V}^{\vee}\to\mathbb{Z}(1)
.
Then
\bm{\lambda}:\mathcal{Q}\to\mathcal{Q}^{\vee}
induces a pairing
\psi:\mathfrak{V}\times\mathfrak{V}\to\mathbb{Z}(1)
by defining
\psi:=\Phi^{\mathrm{can}}\circ(\mathrm{Id},T_{\mathbb{Z}}\bm{\lambda})
. Its base change to
V:=\mathfrak{V}\otimes\mathbb{Q}
is a pairing
\psi:V\times V\to\mathbb{Q}(1)
. By
[
18
, Rmk. 10.2.4]
,
\psi
is an alternating pairing.

The weight
-2
graded piece
\mathfrak{V}_{-2}:=\gr^{W}_{-2}\mathfrak{V}\cong H_{1}(T,\mathbb{Z})
is a totally isotropic space of
\mathfrak{V}
with respect to
\psi
, whose orthogonal complement is
\mathfrak{V}^{\perp}=W_{-1}\mathfrak{V}
. The alternating pairing
\psi
induces a pairing
\psi^{et}:\mathfrak{V}_{0}\times\mathfrak{V}_{-2}\to\mathbb{Z}(1)
, where
\mathfrak{V}_{0}:=\gr^{W}_{0}\mathfrak{V}\cong Y
; note that
\psi^{et}
does not depend on the choice of liftings for
\mathfrak{V}_{0}
.
Moreover, since
\psi(\mathfrak{V}_{-2},\mathfrak{V}^{\perp})=0
, there is a canonical alternating pairing
\psi^{ab}:\mathfrak{V}_{-1}\times\mathfrak{V}_{-1}\to\mathbb{Z}(1)
, defined by projecting to
\mathfrak{V}_{-1}:=\gr^{W}_{-1}\mathfrak{V}\cong H_{1}(A,\mathbb{Z})
of the restriction of
\psi
to
\mathfrak{V}_{-1}
. By Riemann’s Theorem,
\psi^{ab}
is a polarization of
\mathfrak{V}_{-1}
, an integral Hodge structure of type
\{(-1,0),(0,-1)\}
.

Note that
\psi^{et}
defines an embedding
Y\cong\mathfrak{V}_{0}\hookrightarrow X\cong\mathfrak{V}_{-2}^{\vee}
with finite cokernel, which we abusively denote by
\psi^{et}
. Together with
\psi^{ab}
, and by Riemann’s Theorem, the pair
(\psi^{et},\psi^{ab})
determines and is determined by the polarization
\bm{\lambda}=(\bm{\lambda}^{et},\bm{\lambda}^{ab})
.
We will call such a
\psi
induced by
\bm{\lambda}
a
polarization
of the integral mixed Hodge structure
\mathfrak{V}
.

Lemma 2.43
.

Let us fix
(V,\psi)
and a cusp label representative
\Phi^{\ddagger}=(Q^{\ddagger},X^{{\ddagger},+},g^{\ddagger})
of
G^{\ddagger}
. Fix a
\mathbb{Z}
-lattice
\mathfrak{V}\subset V
. Let
\mathbb{M}_{\mathrm{MH}}(\mathfrak{V})
be an integral mixed Hodge structure defined by
x^{\ddagger}\in D_{\Phi^{\ddagger}}^{+}
.
Suppose that there is another pair
(\mathbb{M}_{\mathrm{MH}}(\mathfrak{U}),\psi_{0})
of integral mixed Hodge structures of type
\{(0,0);(-1,0),(0,-1);(-1,-1)\}
with polarization
\psi_{0}
.
Suppose that there is an isomorphism of lattices
f:\mathfrak{V}\to\mathfrak{U}
, which matches
\psi
and
\psi_{0}
, and matches the weight filtrations of
\mathfrak{V}
and
\mathfrak{U}
.

Then there is
g\in P_{\Phi^{\ddagger}}(\mathbb{R})U_{\Phi^{\ddagger}}(\mathbb{C})
, such that the mixed Hodge structure on
V_{\mathbb{C}}:=\mathfrak{V}\otimes\mathbb{C}
induced by the action of
g
on the mixed Hodge structure
\mathbb{M}_{\mathrm{MH}}(\mathfrak{V})\otimes\mathbb{C}
coincides with the pullback of the mixed Hodge structure on
U_{\mathbb{C}}:=\mathfrak{U}\otimes\mathbb{C}
to
V_{\mathbb{C}}
under
f
.

Proof.

Denote by
\mathfrak{V}_{-i}
and
\mathfrak{U}_{-i}
for
i=0,1,2
the graded pieces of
\mathfrak{V}
and
\mathfrak{U}
, with respect to the corresponding weights
-i
. Denote by
W^{\ddagger}
the unipotent radical of
Q^{\ddagger}
,
P^{\ddagger}:=P_{\Phi^{\ddagger}}
and
U^{\ddagger}:=U_{\Phi^{\ddagger}}
. After pulling back the mixed Hodge structure on
U_{\mathbb{C}}
to
V_{\mathbb{C}}
via
f
, we can assume
f=\mathrm{Id}
without loss of generality.
By
[
64
, Prop. 1.16 (b)]
, it suffices to show that there is a
g\in P^{\ddagger}(\mathbb{R})W^{\ddagger}(\mathbb{C})
, such that, the weight filtrations and Hodge filtrations on
V_{\mathbb{C}}
induced by
\mathbb{M}_{\mathrm{MH}}(\mathfrak{V})
and induced by (the pullback of)
\mathbb{M}_{\mathrm{MH}}(\mathfrak{U})
are matched after an action of
g
. The weight filtrations are assumed to be equal; we denote it by
W
.

By
[
16
, Lem. 1.2.11]
, there is a canonical bigrading
A^{p,q}_{V}
for
\mathbf{M}_{\mathrm{MH}}(V)
, such that,
V_{\mathbb{C}}=\bigoplus\limits_{(p,q)=(-1,-1),(-1,0),(0,-1),(0,0)}A^{p,q}_{V}
, and such that
A^{p,q}_{V}
can recover the weight filtration
W_{\bullet}
and the Hodge filtration
F^{\bullet}_{V}
of
V_{\mathbb{C}}
. See also
[
64
, Prop. 1.2]
. In our case, we have:
A^{-1,-1}_{V}=\mathfrak{V}_{-2}\otimes\mathbb{C}
; the projection of
A_{V}^{-1,0}\oplus A_{V}^{0,-1}
to
\gr^{W}_{-1}V_{\mathbb{C}}
is the Hodge decomposition of
\gr^{W}_{-1}V_{\mathbb{C}}
;
A^{0,0}_{V}\cong\gr^{W}_{0}V_{\mathbb{C}}
.
Then the Hodge structure is determined by a homomorphism
h_{V}:\mathbb{S}_{\mathbb{C}}\to\mathrm{GL}(V_{\mathbb{C}})
, such that
(z_{1},z_{2})\mapsto z_{1}^{-p}z_{2}^{-q}
on
A^{p,q}_{V}
. By the same lemma, there are similar complex spaces
A^{p,q}_{U}
for
U_{\mathbb{C}}
with corresponding property, replacing “
V
” with “
U
”. Note that the bigrading pieces
A^{p,q}_{V}
and
A^{p,q}_{U}
are canonically determined by the mixed Hodge structure. To show the statement, it suffices to show that
h_{V}
and
h_{U}
are conjugate under some element
g\in P^{\ddagger}(\mathbb{R})W^{\ddagger}(\mathbb{C})
.

Let
\varsigma_{V}:\bigoplus\limits_{(p,q)}A_{V}^{p,q}\to V_{\mathbb{C}}
be the canonical splitting, and let
\varsigma_{U}
be the corresponding object for
U
. The pullback of
\psi
to
\bigoplus_{(p,q)}A_{U}^{p,q}
(resp.
\bigoplus_{(p,q)}A_{V}^{(p,q)}
) under
\varsigma_{U}
(resp.
\varsigma_{U}
) is a
3\times 3
anti-diagonal blocked matrix. In fact, we can see this via the perfect pairing of the de Rham realizations of
1
-motives over
\mathbb{C}
. See
[
18
, 10.2.7]
and
[
7
]
. Since
A_{V}^{-1,-1}=A_{U}^{-1,-1}=\mathfrak{V}_{-2}\otimes\mathbb{C}
and since
A_{V}^{0,0}=A_{U}^{0,0}=\gr_{0}^{W}V_{\mathbb{C}}
,
\varsigma_{U}^{-1}\circ\varsigma_{V}
matches the
0
-th and
-2
-th graded pieces of the weight filtration
W
.
After taking the
W
-graded pieces
\bigoplus\limits_{i=-2}^{0}\gr_{i}^{W}V_{\mathbb{C}}
, there is a
\overline{g}\in G_{\Phi^{\ddagger},h}(\mathbb{R})
such that the Hodge filtrations on each graded piece induced by
\mathbb{M}_{\mathrm{MH}}(\mathfrak{V})
and
\mathbb{M}_{\mathrm{MH}}(\mathfrak{U})
are matched after the action of
\overline{g}
. Also, we know that
\overline{g}
lifts to an element
p\in P^{\ddagger}(\mathbb{R})
.
Then after replacing
\varsigma_{V}
with
p\circ\varsigma_{V}
, we can identify
A_{V}^{p,q}
with
A_{U}^{p,q}
for each
(p,q)
. We can also choose an identification
\bigoplus_{(p,q)}A^{p,q}_{U}\cong\bigoplus_{(p,q)}A^{p,q}_{V}
such that it matches
\varsigma_{U}^{*}\psi
with
\varsigma_{V}^{*}\psi
.
We then see that
\varsigma_{U}^{-1}\circ\varsigma_{V}
corresponds to a matrix in
W^{\ddagger}(\mathbb{C})
.
∎

2.4.3.

With the background in previous subsections, we can summarize the desired moduli interpretation now.
Recall that
\square
is a finite set of prime numbers, which, in our case, is
\{p\}
or
\emptyset
. Let us fix a cusp label representative
\Phi^{\ddagger}=(Q^{\ddagger},X^{{\ddagger},+},g^{\ddagger})
of
G^{\ddagger}
.
Recall that in §
2.1
that
(V_{\mathbb{Z}},\psi_{\mathbb{Z}})
is a pair of
\mathbb{Z}
-lattice
V_{\mathbb{Z}}
with
\psi_{\mathbb{Z}}:V_{\mathbb{Z}}\times V_{\mathbb{Z}}\to\mathbb{Z}(1)
, the restriction of
\psi
to
V_{\mathbb{Z}}
which takes its values in
\mathbb{Z}(1)
.

Let
\bm{\xi}_{{(V_{\mathbb{Z}},\psi_{\mathbb{Z}})},\Phi^{\ddagger},K^{{\ddagger},\square}}:(Sch/\mathbb{Z}_{(\square)})^{\mathrm{op}}\to(Grpds)
be a moduli problem of isomorphism classes of
1
-motives with additional structures, which can be described as follows:

For any locally Noetherian and connected
\mathbb{Z}_{(\square)}
-scheme
S
,
\bm{\xi}_{(V_{\mathbb{Z}},\psi_{\mathbb{Z}}),\Phi^{\ddagger},K^{{\ddagger},\square}}(S)
is a groupoid whose objects consist of the tuples
(\mathcal{Q},\bm{\lambda},[u]_{(K^{\square}_{\Phi^{\ddagger}})^{g^{\ddagger}}})
, where

•

(\mathcal{Q},\bm{\lambda})
is a polarized
1
-motive over
S
such that
\underline{X}_{\mathcal{Q}}
and
\underline{Y}_{\mathcal{Q}}
split, and

•

[u]_{(K^{\square}_{\Phi^{\ddagger}})^{g^{\ddagger}}}\in\Gamma(S,\isom(V_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{\square}},T^{\square}\mathcal{Q})/{(K^{\square}_{\Phi^{\ddagger}})}^{g^{\ddagger}})
is a level structure of type
(V_{\mathbb{Z}},\psi_{\mathbb{Z}},\Phi^{\ddagger},K^{{\ddagger},\square})
. See Definition
2.38
.

Any pair of such tuples
(\mathcal{Q},\bm{\lambda},[u]_{(K^{\square}_{\Phi^{\ddagger}})^{g^{\ddagger}}})
and
(\mathcal{Q}^{\prime},\bm{\lambda}^{\prime},[u^{\prime}]_{(K^{\square}_{\Phi^{\ddagger}})^{g^{\ddagger}}})
are equivalent in
\bm{\xi}_{(V_{\mathbb{Z}},\psi_{\mathbb{Z}}),\Phi^{\ddagger},K^{{\ddagger},\square}}(S)
, denoted as
(\mathcal{Q},\bm{\lambda},[u]_{(K^{\square}_{\Phi^{\ddagger}})^{g^{\ddagger}}})\sim_{\mathrm{iso}}(\mathcal{Q}^{\prime},\bm{\lambda}^{\prime},[u^{\prime}]_{(K^{\square}_{\Phi^{\ddagger}})^{g^{\ddagger}}})
, if there is an isomorphism
f:\mathcal{Q}\to\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}
such that
f^{\vee}\circ\lambda^{\prime}\circ f=\lambda
and such that
T^{\square}f\circ u=u^{\prime}
modulo the action of
(K^{\square}_{\Phi^{\ddagger}})^{g^{\ddagger}}
. This also describes the objects in
\bm{\xi}_{(V_{\mathbb{Z}},\psi_{\mathbb{Z}}),\Phi^{\ddagger},K^{{\ddagger},\square}}(S)
for a general object
S
in
\ob(Sch/\mathbb{Z}_{(\square)})
, since objects
(\mathcal{Q},\bm{\lambda},[u]_{(K^{\square}_{\Phi^{\ddagger}})^{g^{\ddagger}}})
are finitely presented.

In fact, for any neat open compact
K^{\ddagger}
,
\xi_{(V_{\mathbb{Z}},\psi_{\mathbb{Z}}),\Phi^{\ddagger},K^{{\ddagger}},\mathbb{C}}
is representable by
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi^{\ddagger}},\mathbb{C}}
. Let us explain this now.

Lemma 2.44
.

Let
(\mathcal{Q},\bm{\lambda})
be a polarized
1
-motive over a
\mathbb{Z}_{(\square)}
-scheme
S
. Let
\mathcal{U}\subset V^{\square}\mathcal{Q}
be an open compact subgroup of
V^{\square}\mathcal{Q}
such that
\mathcal{U}_{\overline{s}}
is
\pi_{1}(S,\overline{s})
-invariant for any
\overline{s}\in S
. Then there is a
1
-motive
\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}
with a
\mathbb{Z}_{(\square)}^{\times}
-isogeny
f:\mathcal{Q}\to\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}
and a
\mathbb{Z}_{(\square)}^{\times}
-polarization
\bm{\lambda}^{\prime}:\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}\to\mathcal{Q}^{\prime,\vee}
such that
f^{\vee}\circ\bm{\lambda}^{\prime}\circ f=\bm{\lambda}
.

Proof.

This is similar to Construction
2.4
; see also
[
61
, Lem. 4.22]
and
[
44
, Cor. 1.3.5.4]
. In fact, there is a prime-to-
\square
positive integer
N
such that
{N}T^{\square}\mathcal{Q}\subset\mathcal{U}
. Since
\frac{1}{N}\mathcal{U}/T^{\square}\mathcal{Q}
is a finite prime-to-
\square
-torsion subgroup of
\mathcal{Q}
, define a homomorphism between
1
-motives
g:\mathcal{Q}\to\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}
to be the quotient of
\mathcal{Q}
by
\frac{1}{N}\mathcal{U}/T^{\square}\mathcal{Q}
.
The quotient is a
1
-motive by Lemma
2.14
. Then
f:\mathcal{Q}\xleftarrow{[N]}\mathcal{Q}\xrightarrow{g}\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}
is the desired
\mathbb{Z}_{(\square)}^{\times}
-isogeny.

For the second sentence, let
\bm{\lambda}^{\prime}:=f^{\vee,-1}\circ\bm{\lambda}\circ f^{-1}
. See Appendix
B
for the definition of inverse
\mathbb{Z}_{(\square)}^{\times}
-isogenies.
∎

Then we can associate with any
\wp=[(x,p)]\in\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi^{\ddagger}}}(\mathbb{C})
a polarized
1
-motive
(\mathcal{Q}_{\wp},\bm{\lambda}_{\wp})
with a level structure
[u]_{(K_{\Phi^{\ddagger}}^{\square})^{g^{\ddagger}}}
.

Construction 2.45
.

(1) Since
(V_{\mathbb{Z}},\psi_{\mathbb{Z}})
is fixed,
x
determines an integral mixed Hodge structure
\mathbb{M}_{\mathrm{MH}}(x)
of
V_{\mathbb{Z}}
. Then
\psi
or
-\psi
is a polarization of
\mathbb{M}_{\mathrm{MH}}(x)
, which depends on
x
is in
D_{\Phi^{\ddagger}}^{+}
or not. Since
P_{\Phi^{\ddagger}}(\mathbb{R})
acts transitively on
\pi_{0}(D_{\Phi^{\ddagger}})
, we can assume that
x\in D_{\Phi^{\ddagger}}^{+}
. Then
x
determines a polarized
1
-motive
(\mathcal{Q}_{x},\bm{\lambda}_{x})
such that there is an isomorphism
\alpha_{x}:T_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathcal{Q}_{x}\xrightarrow{\sim}V_{\mathbb{Z}}
; for a different
x^{\prime}
in
P_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})
-orbit of
x
,
\alpha_{x^{\prime}}
and
\alpha_{x}
differ by an action of
P_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})
. That is, if
t\in P_{\Phi^{\ddagger}}(\mathbb{Q})
and
tx=x^{\prime}
, there is a commutative diagram

(2.27)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathcal{Q}_{x}\otimes\mathbb{Q}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V_{\mathbb{Q}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathcal{Q}_{x^{\prime}}\otimes\mathbb{Q}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V_{\mathbb{Q}},}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha_{x}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha_{x^{\prime}}}

such that
V_{\mathbb{Z}}
corresponds to
T_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathcal{Q}_{x}
(resp.
T_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathcal{Q}_{x^{\prime}}
) under
\alpha_{x}
(resp.
\alpha_{x^{\prime}}
).

Let us fix a choice of
x\in D_{\Phi^{\ddagger}}^{+}
and
p\in P_{\Phi^{\ddagger}}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
. Then there is a left-multiplication

(2.28)

c_{p^{{\ddagger},\square}}:(V_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{\square}},\psi_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{\square}})\xrightarrow{p^{{\ddagger},\square}}(V_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{\square}}^{(p^{{\ddagger},\square})},\nu(p^{{\ddagger},\square})^{-1}\psi_{\mathbb{A}_{f}^{\square}}|_{V_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{\square}}^{(p^{{\ddagger},\square})}}),

where
p^{{\ddagger},\square}
is the
\mathbb{A}_{f}^{\square}
-component of
p{g^{\ddagger}}
.
Then
c_{p^{{\ddagger},\square}}V_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{\square}}
maps to an open compact subgroup of
V_{\mathbb{A}_{f}^{\square}}\xrightarrow{\alpha^{-1}_{x}}T_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathcal{Q}_{x}\otimes\mathbb{A}_{f}^{\square}\cong V^{\square}\mathcal{Q}_{x}
. By Lemma
2.44
, there is a
1
-motive
\mathcal{Q}_{\wp}
and a
\mathbb{Z}_{(\square)}^{\times}
-isogeny
f_{x}:\mathcal{Q}_{x}\to\mathcal{Q}_{\wp}
, such that
V^{\square}f_{x}\circ c_{p^{{\ddagger},\square}}(V_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{\square}})=T^{\square}\mathcal{Q}_{\wp}
. Note that
\mathcal{Q}_{\wp}
indeed only depends on
\wp
but not the choice of liftings
(x,p)
. Let
u_{\wp}:=V^{\square}f_{x}\circ c_{p^{{\ddagger},\square}}|_{V_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{\square}}}
. Then the

(K^{\square}_{\Phi^{\ddagger}})^{g^{\ddagger}}
-orbit
u^{\square}:=[u_{\wp}]_{(K_{\Phi^{\ddagger}}^{\square})^{g^{\ddagger}}}
is the desired level structure.

Note that since this process determines a
\mathbb{Z}_{(\square)}^{\times}
-polarization
\bm{\lambda}^{\prime}
by
loc. cit.
, there is a unique polarization
\bm{\lambda}_{\wp}:=r(p^{{\ddagger},\square})^{-1}\bm{\lambda}^{\prime}
for
\mathcal{Q}_{\wp}
, such that,
u^{\square}
sends
\psi_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{\square}}
to a
\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{\square,\times}
-multiple of

.

(2) Conversely, let us fix any object
(\mathcal{Q},\bm{\lambda},[u]_{(K_{\Phi^{\ddagger}}^{\square})^{g^{\ddagger}}})
over
\mathbb{C}
.
There is a map
\alpha:T_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathcal{Q}\xrightarrow{\sim}V^{(g^{\ddagger})}_{\mathbb{Z}}
, such that
\gr_{0}\alpha=\alpha_{0}^{(g^{\ddagger}),-1}
and
\gr_{-2}\alpha=\alpha_{-2}^{(g^{\ddagger}),-1}

corresponds to
\psi_{\mathbb{Z}}^{(g^{\ddagger})}
under

.
In fact, fix a splitting

and a splitting
\zeta_{\mathbb{Z}}:\gr_{\bullet}^{W}V_{\mathbb{Z}}^{(g^{\ddagger})}\to V_{\mathbb{Z}}^{(g^{\ddagger})}

is the filtration defined by
Q^{\ddagger}
. Then there is such an
\alpha
, since there is an
\alpha_{-1}:\gr_{-1}T_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathcal{Q}\to\gr^{W}_{-1}V_{\mathbb{Z}}
sending
\bm{\lambda}^{ab}
to
\psi^{(g^{\ddagger}),ab}_{\mathbb{Z}}
and since
\alpha_{0}^{(g^{\ddagger})}
and

are determined by the definition of level structures. By Lemma

, we find a point
x\in D_{\Phi^{\ddagger}}
, which determines an isomorphism of integral mixed Hodge structures

.

Consider the diagram

(2.29)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(V_{\mathbb{A}_{f}^{\square}},\psi,Z^{(g^{\ddagger})}_{\mathbb{A}_{f}^{\square}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V^{\square}\mathcal{Q}\cong T_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathcal{Q}\otimes\mathbb{A}_{f}^{\square}}

Hence, by Construction

and Lemma

,
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi^{\ddagger}}}(\mathbb{C})\cong\bm{\xi}_{(V_{\mathbb{Z}},\psi_{\mathbb{Z}}),\Phi^{\ddagger},K^{{\ddagger}}}(\mathbb{C})
.

On the other hand, by
[

, 2.2.14-2.2.17]
, or by Proposition

and the proof of

, Prop. 6.2.4.7]

,
\bm{\xi}_{(V_{\mathbb{Z}},\psi_{\mathbb{Z}}),\Phi^{\ddagger},K^{{\ddagger},\square}}
is representable by a separated smooth scheme
\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi^{\ddagger}},\mathbb{Z}_{(\square)}}(P_{\Phi^{\ddagger}},D_{\Phi^{\ddagger}})
over
\mathbb{Z}_{(\square)}
. Write ??
:=\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi^{\ddagger}},{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}(P_{\Phi^{\ddagger}},D_{\Phi^{\ddagger}})
.

We then have
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi^{\ddagger}},\mathbb{C}}\cong\bm{\xi}_{(V_{\mathbb{Z}},\psi_{\mathbb{Z}}),\Phi^{\ddagger},K^{{\ddagger}},\mathbb{C}}.
Furthermore,
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi^{\ddagger}},\mathbb{Q}}\cong\bm{\xi}_{(V_{\mathbb{Z}},\psi_{\mathbb{Z}}),\Phi^{\ddagger},K^{{\ddagger}},\mathbb{Q}}
as
\mathbb{Q}
-schemes, by

.

2.4.4.

Recall that
\Phi_{0}=(Q,X^{+}_{0},g_{0})
maps to
\Phi^{\ddagger}=(Q^{\ddagger},X^{{\ddagger},+},g^{\ddagger})
, that is,

Q\subset Q^{\ddagger}
,
X_{0}^{+}\subset X^{{\ddagger},+}
, and
g_{0}=g^{\ddagger}
.

The embedding
\iota:(G_{0},X_{0})\hookrightarrow(G^{\ddagger},X^{\ddagger})
induces a morphism between mixed Shimura data
\iota_{\Phi_{0}}:(P_{\Phi_{0}},D_{\Phi_{0}})\to(P_{\Phi^{\ddagger}},D_{\Phi^{\ddagger}})
, which is an embedding by Corollary
1.10
.

Fix a neat open compact
K=K_{0,p}K^{p}_{0}
. We can find a neat open compact
K^{\ddagger}=K_{p}^{\ddagger}K^{p,{\ddagger}}
, such that
K_{0,p}=K^{\ddagger}_{p}\cap G_{0}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
. By Lemma
1.22
,
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi_{0},p}}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi^{\ddagger},p}}
is an embedding.

For integral models, let
K^{\ddagger}
and
K_{0}
be defined as in §
2.1
, and let
\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{0}}}(P_{\Phi_{0}},D_{\Phi_{0}})
be the normalization in
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi_{0}}}
of
\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi^{\ddagger}}}
under the morphism
\iota_{\Phi_{0}}:\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi_{0}}}\to\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi^{\ddagger}}}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi^{\ddagger}}}
. Write ??
:=\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{0}}}(P_{\Phi_{0}},D_{\Phi_{0}})
.
Note that the morphism

\iota_{\Phi_{0}}:\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{0}}}\to\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi^{\ddagger}}}
induced by the normalization is finite, but not a closed embedding in general. When
\Phi_{0}=(G_{0},X_{0}^{+},1)
, a trivial cusp label representative,
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi_{0}}}=\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{0}}
. The normalization in
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{0}}
of
\mathcal{S}_{K^{\ddagger}}
under the morphism
\iota:\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{0}}\to\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{\ddagger}}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{S}_{K^{\ddagger}}
is denoted by ??, and the generic fiber of
\mathcal{S}_{K_{0}}
is
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{0}}
.

Let
v|p
be a place of
E_{0}
over
p
, and let
R
be an
\mathcal{O}_{(v)}:=\mathcal{O}_{E_{0},(v)}
-algebra.
For any choice of neat open compact
K^{{\ddagger},p}
subgroup containing
K^{p}_{0}
,
we can associate an object
(\mathcal{Q},\bm{\lambda},[u]_{(K^{p}_{\Phi^{\ddagger}})^{g^{\ddagger}}})
in
\bm{\xi}_{(V_{\mathbb{Z}},\psi_{\mathbb{Z}}),\Phi^{\ddagger},K^{{\ddagger},p}}(R)
for any
R
-valued point in
\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{0}}}(R)
by pulling back the tautological family via
\iota_{\Phi_{0}}
.
Define
[u]_{(K_{\Phi_{0}}^{p})^{g^{\ddagger}}}:=\varprojlim_{K_{\Phi_{0}}^{p}\subset K_{\Phi^{\ddagger}}^{p}}[u]_{(K_{\Phi^{\ddagger}}^{p})^{g^{\ddagger}}}
. Then we have associated a tuple
(\mathcal{Q},\bm{\lambda},[u]_{(K_{\Phi_{0}}^{p})^{g^{\ddagger}}})
to any

-point. See Construction
2.45
for the case when

.

2.5.
Toroidal compactifications of integral models of Hodge type

For the convenience of the readers, let us briefly summarize Chai and Faltings’, Lan’s and Madapusi’s theorems on toroidal compactifications of integral models of Shimura varieties of Siegel/PEL/Hodge type.

Theorem 2.46

(Faltings-Chai
[
21
]
, for Siegel-type case; Lan
[
44
]
,
[
45
]
and
[
46
]
, for PEL-type case; Madapusi
[
50
]
, for Hodge-type case)
.

Let
(G,X)
be a Shimura datum of Siegel, PEL or Hodge type. Let
\Sigma
be an admissible polyhedral cone decomposition for
\mathrm{Sh}_{K}(G,X)
. Let
K:=K_{p}K^{p}
, where
K_{p}
is open compact in
G(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
and
K^{p}
is neat open compact in
G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
. Fix a place
v|p
of the reflex field
E
of
(G,X)
. For different cases, we have the following assumptions for
\Sigma
:

(1)

If
(G,X)
is of Siegel or PEL type, we assume that
\Sigma
is any smooth or projective cone decomposition.

(2)

If
(G,X)
is of Hodge type, we fix an embedding:
\iota:(G,X)\hookrightarrow(G^{\ddagger},X^{\ddagger})
. We assume that
\Sigma
is a refinement of an admissible cone decomposition
\Sigma^{\prime}
for
\mathrm{Sh}_{K}
, where
\Sigma^{\prime}
is induced by a smooth, or smooth and projective cone decomposition
\Sigma^{\ddagger}
for
\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{\ddagger}}
.

Under the assumptions for
\Sigma
as above, we have the following statements:

(1)

There is a normal algebraic space
\mathcal{S}_{K}^{\Sigma}
that is proper over
\mathcal{O}_{E,(v)}
extending the toroidal compactification
\mathrm{Sh}_{K}^{\Sigma}
over
E
constructed by
[
64
]
. The algebraic space
\mathcal{S}_{K}^{\Sigma}
is a projective scheme if
\Sigma
is induced by a projective
\Sigma^{\ddagger}
; if
\Sigma
is smooth,
\mathcal{S}_{K}^{\Sigma}
is smooth if and only if
\mathcal{S}_{K}
is smooth.

(2)

We have a good stratification
\mathcal{S}^{\Sigma}_{K}=\coprod\limits_{\Upsilon}\mathcal{Z}_{\Upsilon,K}
, where each
\mathcal{Z}_{\Upsilon,K}
is a locally closed normal subspace of
\mathcal{S}^{\Sigma}_{K}
that is flat over
\mathcal{O}_{E,(v)}
. The strata ??are indexed by the same set of cusp labels with cones with elements of the form
\Upsilon=[(\Phi,\sigma)]\in\mathrm{Cusp}_{K}(G,X,\Sigma)
defined in the characteristic zero theory. Moreover,
\overline{\mathcal{Z}}_{\Upsilon,K}=\coprod\limits_{\Upsilon^{\prime}\leq\Upsilon}\mathcal{Z}_{\Upsilon^{\prime},K}
.

(3)

Fix any
\Upsilon=[(\Phi,\sigma)]
. There is an integral model
\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi}}
of the boundary mixed Shimura variety
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi}}
, defined over
\mathcal{O}_{E,(v)}
. There is a tower
\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi}}\to\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{K_{\Phi}}\to\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi},h}
over
\mathcal{O}_{E,(v)}
extending the tower
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi}}\to\overline{\mathrm{Sh}}_{K_{\Phi}}\to\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi},h}
over
E
, where the first morphism is an
\mathbf{E}_{K_{\Phi}}
-torsor and the second morphism is proper. Denote by
\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi}}(\sigma)
the toric scheme defined by the twisted affine torus embedding
\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi}}(\sigma):=\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi}}\times^{\mathbf{E}_{K_{\Phi}}}\mathbf{E}_{K_{\Phi}}(\sigma)
. Denote by
\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi},\sigma}
the
\sigma
-stratum of
\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi}}(\sigma)
.

(4)

There is an isomorphism
\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi},\sigma}\cong\mathcal{Z}_{[({\Phi,\sigma})],K}
between the
\sigma
-stratum in
\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi}}(\sigma)
and the stratum
\mathcal{Z}_{[(\Phi,\sigma)],K}
in
\mathcal{S}^{\Sigma}_{K}
.
Moreover, there is a strata-preserving isomorphism

{(\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi}}(\sigma))}{}^{\wedge{\ }}_{\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi},\sigma}}\cong{(\mathcal{S}^{\Sigma}_{K})}{}^{\wedge{\ }}_{\mathcal{Z}_{[({\Phi,\sigma})],K}}
. Here, “strata-preserving” has the following meaning: For any affine open formal subscheme
\mathfrak{U}=\spf(A,I)
of
{(\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi}}(\sigma))}{}^{\wedge{\ }}_{\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi},\sigma}}\cong{(\mathcal{S}^{\Sigma}_{K})}{}^{\wedge{\ }}_{\mathcal{Z}_{[({\Phi,\sigma})],K}}
which canonically induces morphisms
\mathsf{c}_{1}:\spec A\to\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi}}(\sigma)
and
\mathsf{c}_{2}:\spec A\to\mathcal{S}_{K}^{\Sigma}
, the two stratifications on
\spec A
defined by the pullback of the stratification on
\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi}}(\sigma)
under
\mathsf{c}_{1}
and the pullback of the stratification on
\mathcal{S}_{K}^{\Sigma}
under
\mathsf{c}_{2}
coincide.

Convention 2.47
.

To be compatible with the conventions of the previous subsections, when
(G,X)
is of Siegel type, in the theorem above:
(G,X)=(G^{\ddagger},X^{\ddagger})
,
K=K^{\ddagger}
, and all the symbols “
\Phi
” and “
\sigma
” in the theorem above should be added superscripts “
{\ddagger}
”. Similarly, when
(G,X)
of Hodge type, then in the theorem above:
(G,X)=(G_{0},X_{0})
,
K=K_{0}
, and all “
\Phi
” and “
\sigma
” above should be added subscripts
0
. That is,
\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi^{\ddagger}}}
(resp.
\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{0}}}
) introduced in previous subsections are the integral models of boundary mixed Shimura varieties
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi^{\ddagger}}}
(resp.
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi_{0}}}
) described in Part
3
of Theorem
2.46
for the compactification
\mathcal{S}_{K^{\ddagger}}^{\Sigma^{\ddagger}}
(resp.
\mathcal{S}_{K_{0}}^{\Sigma_{0}}
).

The assumptions in
[
21
]
are: (1)
(G,X)=(G^{\ddagger},X^{\ddagger})
is of Siegel type, (2)
K^{\ddagger}=K^{\ddagger}(n)
is a principal
n
level subgroup with
(n,p)=1
, and (3)
\Sigma^{\ddagger}
is smooth with respect to
K^{\ddagger}
(and is admissible, finite, complete and without self-intersections). In
[
44
]
, the
\mathcal{S}_{K}^{\Sigma}
as above is constructed when
(G,X)
is of PEL type,
K=K_{p}K^{p}
is defined as Theorem
2.46
with the assumption that
K_{p}
is hyperspecial, and
\Sigma
is smooth with respect to
K
; the hyperspecial assumption for
K_{p}
is removed in
[
45
]
. In
[
46
]
, the
\mathcal{S}_{K}^{\Sigma}
as above is constructed for any projective
\Sigma
. In
[
50
]
, there is also no assumption on the level
K_{0,p}
at
p
when
(G,X)=(G_{0},X_{0})
is of Hodge type. In fact, with the same settings as in §
2.1
, if
\Sigma=\Sigma_{0}
is induced by some smooth cone decomposition
\Sigma^{\ddagger}
for
(G^{\ddagger},X^{\ddagger})
via the embedding
\iota:(G_{0},X_{0})\hookrightarrow(G^{\ddagger},X^{\ddagger})
, then ??, the toroidal compactification of the integral model
\mathcal{S}_{K_{0}}
associated with the cone decomposition
\Sigma_{0}
, is constructed as the normalization in
\mathrm{Sh}_{K}^{\Sigma}
of the toroidal compactification ??of the Siegel-type integral model
\mathcal{S}_{K^{\ddagger}}
. When
\Sigma^{\prime}_{0}
is a refinement of
\Sigma_{0}
, there is also such a
\mathcal{S}_{K}^{\Sigma_{0}^{\prime}}
as in the theorem above by
[
50
, Rmk. 4.1.6]
.

When
(G,X)=(G^{\ddagger},X^{\ddagger})
is of Siegel type and
K^{\ddagger}
is chosen as in §
2.1
, there is a degenerating family
