(\mathcal{G}^{\ddagger},\lambda^{\ddagger},[\varepsilon_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{p}}]_{K^{{\ddagger},p}})
of type
\mathbf{M}^{\mathrm{iso}}_{(V_{\mathbb{Z}},\psi_{\mathbb{Z}}),K^{{\ddagger},p}}
extending the universal family
(\mathcal{A}^{\ddagger},\lambda^{\ddagger}|_{\mathcal{A}},[\varepsilon_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{p}}]_{K^{{\ddagger},p}})
over
\mathcal{S}_{K^{\ddagger}}
.

As we will work over an extension of the reflex field later, let us record the following result:

Proposition 2.48
.

Let
F
be any finite field extension of
E
. Let
\mathcal{S}_{K,\mathcal{O}_{F}}^{\Sigma}
be the normalization of the base change
\mathcal{S}_{K}^{\Sigma}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{E,(v)}}(\mathcal{O}_{E,(v)}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{E}}\mathcal{O}_{F})
. Then the same results in Theorem
2.46
also hold for
\mathcal{S}^{\Sigma}_{K,\mathcal{O}_{F}}
, with the strata and toric torsors replaced also by the normalizations of their base change from
\mathcal{O}_{E,(v)}
to
\mathcal{O}_{E,(v)}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{E}}\mathcal{O}_{F}
.

Proof.

This can be seen from construction, as the arguments in
[
50
]
still work if the reflex field is replaced with a finite field extension of it, but let us give a direct proof here. We temporarily denote all normalizations of the base changes of
\mathcal{O}_{E,(v)}
-algebraic spaces to
\mathcal{O}_{F}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{E}}\mathcal{O}_{E,(v)}
by adding subscript
\mathcal{O}_{F}
and denote corresponding usual base changes by adding
[\mathcal{O}_{F}]
. The first assertion in Theorem
2.46
is clear; the third one is true because torus parts commute with normalization and base change. There is a quasi-finite morphism
\mathcal{Z}_{\Upsilon,K,\mathcal{O}_{F}}\to\mathcal{S}_{K,\mathcal{O}_{F}}^{\Sigma}
with locally closed image denoted by
[\mathcal{Z}_{\Upsilon}]
; the induced morphism
\mathcal{Z}_{\Upsilon,K,\mathcal{O}_{F}}\to[\mathcal{Z}_{\Upsilon}]
is finite and birational.
We just need to show that
[\mathcal{Z}_{\Upsilon}]
is normal and isomorphic to
\mathcal{Z}_{\Upsilon,K,\mathcal{O}_{F}}
, and that
{(\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi},\mathcal{O}_{F}}(\sigma))}{}^{\wedge{\ }}_{\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi},\mathcal{O}_{F},\sigma}}\cong{(\mathcal{S}^{\Sigma}_{K,\mathcal{O}_{F}})}{}^{\wedge{\ }}_{[\mathcal{Z}_{\Upsilon}]}
.

By
[
40
, Sec. 3.1]
, the assertions except for the normality are true for the usual base change to
\mathcal{O}_{E,(v)}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{E}}\mathcal{O}_{F}
. Let
\bar{x}
be any
\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{p}
-point of
[\mathcal{Z}_{\Upsilon}]
which maps to
\bar{y}
in
\mathcal{Z}_{\Upsilon,K,[\mathcal{O}_{F}]}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{S}_{K,[\mathcal{O}_{F}]}^{\Sigma}
and lifts to an
\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{p}
-point
\bar{x}_{0}
in
\mathcal{Z}_{\Upsilon,K,\mathcal{O}_{F}}
. This induces a finite homomorphism from the complete local ring at
\bar{y}
,
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\bar{y}}
, to the complete local ring at
\bar{x}
,
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\bar{x}}
. Moreover, from
loc. cit.
we have
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\bar{y}}\cong\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi},[\mathcal{O}_{F}]}(\sigma),\bar{y}}
(since pullbacks still have a good description of formal completions).
Suppose that
\bar{y}
maps to a point
\bar{z}
in
\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi},\sigma,[\mathcal{O}_{F}]}
under the canonical projection to
\sigma
-stratum. Let
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{*}_{\bar{z}}
be the normalization in
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\bar{x}}
of the complete local ring
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi},\sigma,[\mathcal{O}_{F}]},\bar{z}}
via the ring homomorphism
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi},\sigma,[\mathcal{O}_{F}]},\bar{z}}\to\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\bar{y}}\cong\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi},[\mathcal{O}_{F}]}(\sigma),\bar{y}}\to\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\bar{x}}
. Then we have
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathcal{Z}_{\Upsilon,K,\mathcal{O}_{F}},\bar{x}_{0}}\cong\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{*}_{\bar{z}}
by Zariski’s main theorem. This implies that
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathcal{Z}_{\Upsilon,K,\mathcal{O}_{F}},\bar{x}_{0}}\cong\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{[\mathcal{Z}_{\Upsilon}],\bar{x}}
; therefore,
[\mathcal{Z}_{\Upsilon}]\cong\mathcal{Z}_{\Upsilon,K,\mathcal{O}_{F}}
. Moreover, we have
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{*}_{\bar{z}}\otimes_{\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi},\sigma,[\mathcal{O}_{F}]},\bar{z}}}\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\bar{y}}\cong\widehat{O}_{\bar{x}}
by Zariski’s main theorem again. Thanks to
[
50
, Lem. A.3.2]
, we now obtain the desired description of the formal completion at the boundary.
∎

3.
Twisting constructions

We continue with the notation in Section
2
. Denote by
G^{\mathrm{ad}}_{0}
the adjoint group of
G_{0}
. Denote by
G^{\mathrm{ad}}_{0}(\mathbb{R})_{1}
the subgroup of
G^{\mathrm{ad}}_{0}(\mathbb{R})
that stabilizes the subspace
X_{0}
in
X_{0}^{\mathrm{ad}}
. Denote
G^{\mathrm{ad}}_{0}(\mathbb{Q})_{1}:=G^{\mathrm{ad}}_{0}(\mathbb{R})_{1}\cap G^{\mathrm{ad}}_{0}(\mathbb{Q})
.

By
[
20
]
, there is a left action of
G^{\mathrm{ad}}_{0}(\mathbb{Q})_{1}
on
\mathrm{Sh}(G_{0},X_{0})
. The goal of this section is to construct “moduli descriptions” (called “twisting” in
[
36
]
and
[
33
]
) in some special cases for this action on (integral models of) compactifications. From §
3.1
to §
3.3
, we extend the twisting construction of Kisin-Pappas
[
33
]
to boundaries. In §
3.4
and in a different setting, we construct a “twisting” related to the quasi-isogeny twisting of Lan
[
45
]
in the Siegel case and conclude that twisting constructions admit extensions to toroidal compactifications (if the cone decompositions are compatible with the actions).

3.1.
Twisting abelian schemes

We explain Kisin and Pappas’ twisting construction for abelian schemes and for algebraic tori. The main difference is that the original construction due to Kisin and Pappas is established on the level of abelian schemes up to prime-to-
p
isogenies.
All materials are from
[
36
, Sec. 3.1 and 3.2]
and
[
33
, Sec. 4.4 and 4.5]
.

3.1.1.

Let
Z
be a faithfully flat affine
group scheme of finite type over
A
, where
A
is a commutative ring. For any
A
-module
M
, a
Z
-action on
M
is defined by a morphism between fppf sheaves
\mathbf{z}:Z\to\underline{\mathrm{Aut}}_{A}M
. Then
\mathbf{z}
induces a map
M\xrightarrow{\mathbf{z}^{*}}M\otimes_{A}\mathcal{O}_{Z}
that endows
M
with an
\mathcal{O}_{Z}
-comodule structure.
Let
\mathcal{P}
be a
Z
-torsor over
A
. Then
M\otimes_{A}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{P}}
is an
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{P}}
-module with semilinear
Z
-action. Then
(M\otimes_{A}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{P}})^{Z}
, the submodule of
N:=M\otimes_{A}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{P}}
fixed by
Z
, can be viewed as the equalizer of the natural
\mathcal{O}_{Z}
-comodule structure
\mathbf{z}^{*}:N\to N\otimes_{A}\mathcal{O}_{Z}
of
N
and the map
p^{*}:N\to N\otimes_{A}\mathcal{O}_{Z}
determined by the natural projection
Z\times_{\spec A}M\to M
.
By
[
33
, Lem. 4.4.3]
,

(3.1)

(M\otimes_{A}\mathcal{O_{P}})^{Z}\otimes_{A}\mathcal{O_{P}}\cong M\otimes_{A}\mathcal{O_{P}}.

Following
[
36
]
and
[
33
]
, define the
twisting of
M
by
\mathcal{P}
by an exact functor

{(-)}^{\mathcal{P}}:M\mapsto{M}^{\mathcal{P}}:=(M\otimes_{A}\mathcal{O_{P}})^{Z},

where the exactness follows from (
3.1
) and the faithful flatness of
Z
(and
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{P}}
) over
A
.

Now let
A={\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}
, let
S
be a
\spec{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}
-scheme and also assume that
Z
is commutative. Set
\mathcal{O}_{F,(p)}:=\mathcal{O}_{F}\otimes\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}
. Since
\mathcal{P}
is flat of finite type over
{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}
, by
[
57
, I, Thm. 1.6]
,
\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{O}_{F,(p)})
is not empty for some finite Galois extension
F
over
\mathbb{Q}
. For this, see also the proof of
[
33
, Lem. 4.4.6]
.

Since
Z(\mathcal{O}_{F,(p)})
acts on
\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{O}_{F,(p)})
freely and transitively, for any
\tilde{\gamma}\in\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{O}_{F,(p)})
and any
\sigma\in\mathrm{Gal}(F/\mathbb{Q})
, there exists a unique
z_{\sigma}^{\tilde{\gamma}}\in Z(\mathcal{O}_{F,(p)})
such that
\sigma\tilde{\gamma}=z_{\sigma}^{\tilde{\gamma}}\cdot\tilde{\gamma}
. Since
z_{\sigma\tau}^{\tilde{\gamma}}\tilde{\gamma}=\sigma\tau\tilde{\gamma}=\sigma(z_{\tau}^{\tilde{\gamma}}\cdot\tilde{\gamma})=\sigma(z_{\tau}^{\tilde{\gamma}})\cdot\sigma\tilde{\gamma}=\sigma(z_{\tau}^{\tilde{\gamma}})\cdot z_{\sigma}^{\tilde{\gamma}}\cdot\tilde{\gamma}
, we have associated with
\tilde{\gamma}
a
1
-cocycle
\sigma\mapsto z_{\sigma}^{\tilde{\gamma}}
.
For another
\tilde{\gamma}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{O}_{F,(p)})
, there is a unique
z\in Z(\mathcal{O}_{F,(p)})
such that
z\tilde{\gamma}=\tilde{\gamma}^{\prime}
, and
\sigma\tilde{\gamma}^{\prime}=\sigma(z)z_{\sigma}^{\tilde{\gamma}}\cdot\tilde{\gamma}=\sigma(z)z^{-1}z_{\sigma}^{\tilde{\gamma}}\cdot\tilde{\gamma}^{\prime}
. This implies that
z_{\sigma}^{\tilde{\gamma}^{\prime}}=\sigma(z)z^{-1}z_{\sigma}^{\tilde{\gamma}}
.

Specializing (
3.1
) at
\tilde{\gamma}
, we have an isomorphism

(3.2)

\iota^{\tilde{\gamma}}:M^{\mathcal{P}}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}\mathcal{O}_{F,(p)}\xrightarrow{\sim}M\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}\mathcal{O}_{F,(p)}.

The Galois group
\mathrm{Gal}(F/\mathbb{Q})
acts on
\mathcal{O}_{F,(p)}
on the left-hand side and acts on both factors on the right-hand side. The action of
\sigma\in\mathrm{Gal}(F/\mathbb{Q})
on
M\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}\mathcal{O}_{F,(p)}
is via
\sigma\mapsto z^{\tilde{\gamma}}_{\sigma}\otimes\sigma\mapsto\mathbf{z}(z^{\tilde{\gamma}}_{\sigma})\otimes\sigma
, and we will omit
\mathbf{z}
if it is clear in the context.
In particular, we have
M^{\mathcal{P}}\cong(M\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}\mathcal{O}_{F,(p)})^{\mathrm{Gal}(F/\mathbb{Q})}
.

3.1.2.

Let
R
be a faithfully flat algebra over
\mathbb{Z}
. Denote
R_{(p)}:=R\otimes{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}
. Suppose that
M
is a free
R_{(p)}
-module of finite rank and that
M_{R}
is an
R
-lattice in
M
.
Let
f^{\tilde{\gamma}}:M_{R}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{F}\otimes{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}\to(M_{R}\otimes{\mathcal{O}_{F}})^{\oplus|\mathrm{Gal}(F/\mathbb{Q})|-1}\otimes{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}
be a homomorphism between
{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}
-modules defined by
f^{\tilde{\gamma}}:=\bigoplus\limits_{\sigma\in\mathrm{Gal}(F/\mathbb{Q})\text{ and }\sigma\neq\mathrm{id}}(z_{\sigma}^{\tilde{\gamma}}\sigma-\mathrm{id})
. Then there is a sufficiently large prime-to-
p
integer
N>0
, such that
Nz_{\sigma}^{\tilde{\gamma}}\sigma
is an endomorphism of
M_{R}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{F}
for all
\sigma\in\mathrm{Gal}(F/\mathbb{Q})
. Define
f^{\tilde{\gamma}}_{N}:=N\circ f^{\tilde{\gamma}}|_{M_{R}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{F}}
, and define
M_{R}^{\tilde{\gamma}}:=\ker f^{\tilde{\gamma}}_{N}
.

Let
V
and
W
be two finite free
R_{(p)}
-modules as
M
above. Suppose that there is an
R_{(p)}
-isomorphism
g:V\to W
. Suppose that
V
(resp.
W
) is equipped with a
Z
-action denoted by
\mathbf{z}^{V}
(resp.
\mathbf{z}^{W}
).
We say
\mathbf{z}^{V}
and
\mathbf{z}^{W}
are compatible with
g
if, for any
{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}
-algebra
R_{0}
and any
z\in Z(\mathcal{O}_{F,(p)})
,
\mathbf{z}^{W}(z)=g\circ\mathbf{z}^{V}(z)\circ g^{-1}\in\mathrm{End}_{R_{(p)}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}R_{0}}(W\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}R_{0})
. We summarize the following observation:

Lemma 3.1
.

Let
V_{R}
(resp.
W_{R}
) be an
R
-lattice of
V
(resp.
W
). Let
g:V_{R}\to W_{R}
be a homomorphism between
R
-lattices of
V
and
W
such that
f\otimes{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}
is an isomorphism. Let
F
be a Galois extension of
\mathbb{Q}
.
Suppose that
W
is equipped with a compatible
Z
-action induced by
g
as above. Fix any
\tilde{\gamma}\in\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{O}_{F,(p)})
. Let
N
be any positive prime-to-
p
integer such that
N\mathbf{z}^{V}(z_{\sigma}^{\tilde{\gamma}})
(resp.
N\mathbf{z}^{W}(z_{\sigma}^{\tilde{\gamma}})
) lies in
\mathrm{End}(V_{R}\otimes{\mathcal{O}_{F}})
(resp.
\mathrm{End}(W_{R}\otimes{\mathcal{O}_{F}})
) for any
\sigma\in\mathrm{Gal}(F/\mathbb{Q})
.
Then there is a unique homomorphism
f_{N}^{W,\tilde{\gamma}}:W_{R}\otimes{\mathcal{O}_{F}}\to W_{R}\otimes{\mathcal{O}_{F}}^{\oplus|\mathrm{Gal}(F/\mathbb{Q})|-1}
such that the diagram

(3.3)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V_{R}\otimes{\mathcal{O}_{F}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W_{R}\otimes{\mathcal{O}_{F}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(V_{R}\otimes{\mathcal{O}_{F}})^{\oplus|\mathrm{Gal}(F/\mathbb{Q})|-1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(W_{R}\otimes{\mathcal{O}_{F}})^{\oplus|\mathrm{Gal}(F/\mathbb{Q})|-1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{N}^{\tilde{\gamma}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{W,\tilde{\gamma}}_{N}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g^{\oplus|\mathrm{Gal}(F/\mathbb{Q})|-1}}

commutes. By taking kernels of the two vertical arrows of the diagram above, there is a natural homomorphism
g^{\tilde{\gamma}}:V^{\tilde{\gamma}}_{R}\to W^{\tilde{\gamma}}_{R}
which induces an isomorphism after tensoring
{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}
. For different choices of
N
,
g^{\tilde{\gamma}}
varies by isomorphisms induced by multiplying prime-to-
p
integers.

If
g
is defined by multiplying
r\in R^{\times}
, then so is
g^{\tilde{\gamma}}
.

Proof.

In fact, we let
f^{W,{\tilde{\gamma}}}_{N}:=\bigoplus\limits_{\sigma\in\mathrm{Gal}(F/\mathbb{Q})\text{ and }\sigma\neq\mathrm{id}}((N\mathbf{z}^{W}(z_{\sigma}^{\tilde{\gamma}})\sigma-N\cdot\mathrm{id})
since
\mathbf{z}^{V}
and
\mathbf{z}^{W}
are compatible. The homomorphism
g^{\tilde{\gamma}}\otimes{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}
is an isomorphism since
g\otimes{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}
is so.
∎

3.1.3.

Denote by
S
a
{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}
-scheme and denote by
\mathcal{A}
a commutative group scheme over
S
. Let
\mathcal{N}
be a finite free
\mathbb{Z}
-algebra. Suppose that there is an
\mathcal{N}
-action on
\mathcal{A}
, that is, there is a homomorphism
m:\mathcal{N}\to\underline{\mathrm{End}}_{S}(\mathcal{A})
. Let
\mathcal{M}
be a finite
\mathcal{N}
-module. Define
Serre’s construction
to be
\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}_{\mathcal{N}}(\mathcal{M},\mathcal{A})^{\circ}
, the fiberwise identity component of the étale sheaf
\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}_{\mathcal{N}}(\mathcal{M},\mathcal{A})
.

If
\mathcal{A}
is an abelian scheme,
\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}_{\mathcal{N}}(\mathcal{M},\mathcal{A})^{\circ}
is representable by an abelian scheme over
S
. When
\mathcal{M}
is a finite projective
\mathcal{N}
-module,
\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}_{\mathcal{N}}(\mathcal{M},\mathcal{A})
is fiberwise connected and therefore is representable by an abelian scheme over
S
. See, e.g.,
[
44
, Prop. 5.2.3.9]
.

Assuming that
\mathcal{M}
is finite projective, we can write
\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{M}^{\vee}}:=\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}_{\mathcal{N}}(\mathcal{M},\mathcal{A})\cong\mathcal{M}^{\vee}\otimes_{\mathcal{N}}\mathcal{A}
, where
\mathcal{M}^{\vee}:=\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}_{\mathcal{N}}(\mathcal{M},\mathcal{N})
. For any scheme
T
over
S
,
\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{M}^{\vee}}(T)=\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{N}}(\mathcal{M},\mathcal{A}(T))\cong\mathcal{M}^{\vee}\otimes_{\mathcal{N}}\mathcal{A}(T)
.

For our purpose, we can further assume that
\mathcal{N}=\mathbb{Z}
and that
\mathcal{M}
is a finite free
\mathbb{Z}
-algebra of finite rank. Then
\mathcal{F}^{\mathcal{M}^{\vee}}:=\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}(\mathcal{M},\mathcal{F})=\mathcal{F}\otimes\mathcal{M}^{\vee}
for any fppf sheaf
\mathcal{F}
over
S
; this is just
\mathcal{F}^{\oplus r}
equipped with an action of
\mathcal{M}^{\vee}
(or rather,
\mathcal{M}
). In this case,
\mathcal{F}^{\mathcal{M}^{\vee}}
is representable by a torus (resp. an étale locally constant sheaf of finite rank, resp. an abelian scheme, resp. a semi-abelian scheme) if
\mathcal{F}
is representable by a torus (resp. an étale locally constant sheaf of finite rank, resp. an abelian scheme, resp. a semi-abelian scheme). If
\mathcal{Q}=[\underline{Y}\to\mathcal{G}^{\natural}]
is a
1
-motive, so is
\mathcal{Q}^{\mathcal{M}^{\vee}}:=[\underline{Y}^{\mathcal{M}^{\vee}}\to\mathcal{G}^{\natural,\mathcal{M}^{\vee}}]
.

Now suppose that we are given a
1
-motive
\mathcal{Q}
and a finite Galois extension
F
of
\mathbb{Q}
. There is a canonical perfect reduced trace pairing between
\mathcal{O}_{F}
and its inverse different
\mathrm{Diff}^{-1}_{\mathcal{O}_{F}/\mathbb{Z}}
:

\mathrm{Tr}_{F/\mathbb{Q}}:\mathcal{O}_{F}\times\mathrm{Diff}^{-1}_{\mathcal{O}_{F}/\mathbb{Z}}\to\mathbb{Z}.

This perfect pairing induces an isomorphism
\mathcal{O}_{F}^{\vee}:=\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathbb{Z}}(\mathcal{O}_{F},\mathbb{Z})\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathrm{Diff}^{-1}_{\mathcal{O}_{F}/\mathbb{Z}}\subset F.

Let
\bm{\lambda}:\mathcal{Q}\to\mathcal{Q}^{\vee}
be an isogeny.
Then

\bm{\lambda}\otimes 1:\mathcal{Q}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{F}\longrightarrow\mathcal{Q}^{\vee}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{F}

is an isogeny. Any embedding of
\mathbb{Z}
-modules
i_{F}:\mathcal{O}_{F}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{O}_{F}^{\vee}

induces an isogeny

(\bm{\lambda}\otimes 1)(i_{F}):\mathcal{Q}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{F}\xrightarrow{\bm{\lambda}\otimes 1}\mathcal{Q}^{\vee}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{F}\xrightarrow{i_{F}}\mathcal{Q}^{\vee}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{F}^{\vee}

between
\mathcal{Q}\otimes{\mathcal{O}_{F}}
and its Cartier dual
\mathcal{Q}^{\vee}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{F}^{\vee}
.

3.1.4.

Let
F
,
\tilde{\gamma}
and
z^{\tilde{\gamma}}_{\sigma}
be defined as in §
3.1.1
. Let
\mathcal{A}
be an abelian scheme or an algebraic torus over
S
. Let
[\mathcal{A}]:=\mathcal{A}\otimes{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}
: it is the sheaf that represents
\mathcal{A}
up to prime-to-
p
isogenies. More precisely, for any
S
-scheme
T
, define the
T
-value points of
[\mathcal{A}]
to be
[\mathcal{A}](T):=\mathrm{Hom}_{S}(T,\mathcal{A})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}
.
Assume that
Z
acts on
[\mathcal{A}]
via a morphism
\mathbf{z}:Z\to\underline{\mathrm{Aut}}_{{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}([\mathcal{A}])
: For any
{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}
-algebra
R
, there is a homomorphism
\mathbf{z}(R):Z(R)\to\underline{\mathrm{Aut}}_{{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}([\mathcal{A}])(R):=(\mathrm{End}_{S}(\mathcal{A})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}R)^{\times}
.
From the discussion above, we have a map
\iota_{\mathrm{Gal}}:\mathrm{Gal}(F/\mathbb{Q})\to\underline{\mathrm{Aut}}_{{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}([\mathcal{A}])(\mathcal{O}_{F,(p)})
defined by
\sigma\mapsto z^{\tilde{\gamma}}_{\sigma}\mapsto\mathbf{z}(z^{\tilde{\gamma}}_{\sigma})
.
The homomorphism
\mathbf{z}(\mathcal{O}_{F,(p)}):Z({\mathcal{O}_{F,(p)}})\to\underline{\mathrm{Aut}}_{{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}([\mathcal{A}])({\mathcal{O}_{F,(p)}})
is equivariant with respect to the actions induced by the Galois action of
\mathrm{Gal}(F/\mathbb{Q})
on
\mathcal{O}_{F}
.

Since
\mathcal{O}_{F}
is finite free over
\mathbb{Z}
,
\underline{\mathrm{Aut}}_{{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}([\mathcal{A}])(\mathcal{O}_{F,(p)})=(\mathrm{End}_{S}(\mathcal{A})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathcal{O}_{F}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}})^{\times}\cong(\mathrm{End}_{S}(\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{O}_{F}})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}})^{\times}\cong\underline{\mathrm{Aut}}_{{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}([\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{O}_{F}}])({\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}})
. So the map
\iota_{\mathrm{Gal}}
above is equivalent to a map

\iota_{\mathrm{Gal}}:\mathrm{Gal}(F/\mathbb{Q})\longrightarrow\underline{\mathrm{Aut}}_{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}([\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{O}_{F}}])({\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}).

The isomorphism (
3.1
) can be read as

[\mathcal{A}]^{\mathcal{P}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{P}}\cong[\mathcal{A}]\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{P}}.

Evaluating at
\tilde{\gamma}
, we have an isomorphism between fppf sheaves over
S
:

(3.4)

\iota^{\tilde{\gamma}}:[\mathcal{A}]^{\mathcal{P}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}\mathcal{O}_{F,(p)}\cong[\mathcal{A}]\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}\mathcal{O}_{F,(p)}.

As discussed in §
3.1.1
, we have that
[\mathcal{A}]^{\mathcal{P}}\cong(\mathcal{A}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{F,(p)})^{\mathrm{Gal}(F/\mathbb{Q})}.

Since
\mathrm{Gal}(F/\mathbb{Q})
is a finite group, there is a positive prime-to-
p
integer
N
such that
N\iota_{\mathrm{Gal}}(\sigma)\in\mathrm{End}_{S}(\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{O}_{F}})
for all
\sigma\in\mathrm{Gal}(F/\mathbb{Q})
.
There is a homomorphism between abelian schemes or algebraic tori

f^{\tilde{\gamma}}_{N}:\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{O}_{F}}\longrightarrow(\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{O}_{F}})^{\oplus|\mathrm{Gal}(F/\mathbb{Q})|-1},

defined by
f^{\tilde{\gamma}}_{N}:=\bigoplus\limits_{\sigma\in\mathrm{Gal}(F/\mathbb{Q})\text{ and }\sigma\neq\mathrm{id}}((Nz_{\sigma}^{\tilde{\gamma}})\sigma-N\cdot\mathrm{id}).

Define
\mathcal{A}^{\tilde{\gamma}}_{N}:=(\ker f^{\tilde{\gamma}}_{N})^{\circ}
to be the fiberwise (geometrically) identity component of
\ker f^{\tilde{\gamma}}_{N}
. Hence,
\ker f^{\tilde{\gamma}}_{N}
and
\mathcal{A}^{\tilde{\gamma}}_{N}
are representable by closed subgroup schemes of
\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{O}_{F}}
.

Let
f^{\tilde{\gamma}}:\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{O}_{F}}\otimes{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}\to(\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{O}_{F}})^{\oplus|\mathrm{Gal}(F/\mathbb{Q})|-1}\otimes{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}
be a morphism between sheaves defined by
f^{\tilde{\gamma}}:=\bigoplus\limits_{\sigma\in\mathrm{Gal}(F/\mathbb{Q})\text{ and }\sigma\neq\mathrm{id}}(z_{\sigma}^{\tilde{\gamma}}\sigma-\mathrm{id})
. Since
(p,N)=1
,
[\mathcal{A}]^{\mathcal{P}}\cong\ker f^{\tilde{\gamma}}\cong\ker Nf^{\tilde{\gamma}}\cong\ker f^{\tilde{\gamma}}_{N}\otimes{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}
.

For different points in
\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{O}_{F,(p)})
, we have the following lemma:

Lemma 3.2
.

Let
\tilde{\gamma}\in\mathcal{P}({\mathcal{O}_{F,(p)}})
. Suppose that
\tilde{\gamma}^{\prime}=z\tilde{\gamma}
for some
z\in Z({\mathcal{O}_{F,(p)}})
. Let
N^{\prime}
be an integer such that
N^{\prime}z\in\mathrm{End}_{S}(\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{O}_{F}})
and
(N^{\prime},p)=1
.
The homomorphism
N^{\prime}z\in\mathrm{End}_{S}(\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{O}_{F}})
induces a prime-to-
p
isogeny between
\ker f^{{\tilde{\gamma}}^{\prime}}_{N}
and
\ker f^{\tilde{\gamma}}_{N}
.

Proof.

This is true because
(Nz_{\sigma}^{\tilde{\gamma}}\sigma-N)\cdot N^{\prime}z=N^{\prime}z\cdot(Nz^{\tilde{\gamma}^{\prime}}_{\sigma}\sigma-N)
, where
z_{\sigma}^{\tilde{\gamma}^{\prime}}=\sigma(z)z^{-1}z_{\sigma}^{\tilde{\gamma}}
for any
\sigma\in\mathrm{Gal}(F/\mathbb{Q})
.
∎

3.1.5.

Since
[\mathcal{A}]^{\mathcal{P}}\cong\ker f^{\tilde{\gamma}}_{N}\otimes{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}
, the isomorphism (
3.4
) is the base change to
{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}
of the following composition of morphisms between schemes

\iota^{\tilde{\gamma}}_{N}:\ker f^{\tilde{\gamma}}_{N}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{F}\longrightarrow\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{O}_{F}}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{F}\longrightarrow\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{O}_{F}}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{F}}\mathcal{O}_{F}\cong\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{O}_{F}},

where the first arrow is induced by the embedding
\ker f^{\tilde{\gamma}}_{N}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{O}_{F}}
and the second arrow is induced by the multiplication of
\mathcal{O}_{F}
to the second factor of
\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{O}_{F}}\cong\mathcal{A}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{F}
.

Now we explain the proof of the following proposition:

Proposition 3.3
.

With the conventions above, the closed subgroup scheme
\mathcal{A}^{\tilde{\gamma}}_{N}
of
\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{O}_{F}}
is an abelian scheme (resp. an algebraic torus) over
S
if
\mathcal{A}
is an abelian scheme (resp. an algebraic torus) over
S
.

Proof.

Since this question is Zariski local on the base, and all the objects and morphisms being studied are finitely presented, we assume that
S
is affine and of finite type over
{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}
.
If
\mathcal{A}
is an abelian scheme, we see that
\mathcal{A}^{\tilde{\gamma}}_{N}
is proper since it is a closed subgroup scheme of
\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{O}_{F}}
, and that it is fiberwise geometrically connected by the construction. Hence, for either case where
\mathcal{A}
is an abelian scheme or an algebraic torus, we only need to show that
\mathcal{A}_{N}^{\tilde{\gamma}}
is smooth. By the fiber-by-fiber criterion of flatness
[
71
,
Lem. 039E
]
, to show the flatness of
f^{\tilde{\gamma}}_{N}
, it suffices to show the fibers of
f^{\tilde{\gamma}}_{N}
at all
x\in S
are flat; by generic flatness
[
71
,
Prop. 052A
]
and the group scheme structures on
\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{O}_{F}}_{x}
, the homomorphisms
f_{\tilde{\gamma},N,x}
are flat.
We see that the homomorphism
f^{\tilde{\gamma}}_{N}
is flat, so is its kernel.
Then we only need to show that
\ker f^{\tilde{\gamma}}_{N}
, or equivalently,
\mathcal{A}_{N}^{\tilde{\gamma}}
, is fiberwise smooth, and it suffices to check it at all closed points of
S
. Note that for any point
x^{\prime}\in S
whose residue field is of characteristic
0
,
\ker f_{N,x^{\prime}}^{\tilde{\gamma}}
and
\mathcal{A}^{\tilde{\gamma}}_{N,x^{\prime}}
are smooth by Cartier’s theorem; see
[
71
,
Lem. 047N
]
.

For any closed point
x\in S
, the residue field
\kappa_{x}
of
x
is perfect. We write
K:=\ker f^{\tilde{\gamma}}_{N}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{F}
and
A:=\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{O}_{F}}
for simplicity. We claim that
\iota^{\tilde{\gamma}}_{N,x}:K_{x}\to A_{x}
, the fiber of
\iota^{\tilde{\gamma}}_{N}
at
x
, is an isogeny, i.e., is surjective with finite kernel. Granting this, we will know that
\ker\iota^{\tilde{\gamma}}_{N,x}
is a finite group. Since
\ker\iota^{\tilde{\gamma}}_{N,x}\otimes{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}
is trivial,
\iota^{\tilde{\gamma}}_{N,x}
is a prime-to-
p
isogeny. Then the identity component of
K_{x}
is an abelian variety or algebraic torus and in particular is smooth. So
K
is smooth. As
K^{\circ}
is isomorphic to a product of copies of
\mathcal{A}^{\tilde{\gamma}}_{N}
, the latter is also smooth.

Now we show the claim in the last paragraph. We apply Lemma
3.4
to
\iota_{\mathrm{red}}:K_{x,\mathrm{red}}^{\circ}\hookrightarrow K_{x}\xrightarrow{\iota_{N,x}^{\tilde{\gamma}}}A_{x}
.
If
(\ker\iota_{\mathrm{red}})_{\mathrm{red}}^{\circ}
is non-trivial, it will have nontrivial
p^{n}
-torsion points since it is an abelian variety or a torus. But this gives us a contradiction since
\ker\iota^{\tilde{\gamma}}_{N,x}\otimes\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}
is trivial. Applying Lemma
3.4
to
f^{\tilde{\gamma}}_{N}
, we see that
\ker\iota^{\tilde{\gamma}}_{N,x}
is finite. Then
\iota^{\tilde{\gamma}}_{N,x}(K):=K/\ker\iota^{\tilde{\gamma}}_{N,x}
can be decomposed as
K_{1}\cdot K^{\prime}
, where
K_{1}
is a finite group and
K^{\prime}
is the image of
K_{x,\mathrm{red}}^{\circ}
in
A_{x}
via
\iota^{\tilde{\gamma}}_{N,x}
. If
K^{\prime}\neq A_{x}
, there is an abelian subvariety or a subtorus
B
in
A_{x}
such that
K^{\prime}\cap B
is finite and
K^{\prime}\cdot B=A
.
Then the cokernel sheaf
\operatorname{coker}\iota^{\tilde{\gamma}}_{N,x}
of
\iota^{\tilde{\gamma}}_{N,x}
is representable by a quotient of
B
by a finite group variety, and therefore is representable by an abelian variety or a torus. Since abelian varieties or tori have non-trivial
p^{n}
-torsion points,
\operatorname{coker}\iota^{\tilde{\gamma}}_{N,x}\otimes{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}
is nontrivial, which leads to a contradiction. So
\iota^{\tilde{\gamma}}_{N,x}
is surjective.
∎

Lemma 3.4
.

Let
\kappa
be a field. Let
A
and
B
be two abelian varieties (resp. two algebraic tori) over
\kappa
. Let
f:A\to B
be a homomorphism with kernel
K
. Let
K_{\mathrm{red}}
be the reduced scheme associated with
K
. Suppose that
K_{\mathrm{red}}
is geometrically reduced (for example, when
\kappa
is perfect), and therefore is endowed with a group scheme structure induced by
K
. Then:

(1)

K
satisfies an extension

0\longrightarrow K_{1}\longrightarrow K\longrightarrow K_{2}\longrightarrow 0,

where
K_{1}
is a finite flat group variety and
K_{2}
is an abelian variety (resp. algebraic torus).

(2)

Denote by
K^{\circ}_{\mathrm{red}}
the identity component of
K_{\mathrm{red}}
. Then
K^{\circ}_{\mathrm{red}}
is an abelian variety (resp. algebraic torus) and the embedding
K_{\mathrm{red}}\hookrightarrow K
induces an isogeny
K_{\mathrm{red}}^{\circ}\to K_{2}
.

Proof.

Without loss of generality, assume that
f
is surjective.
If
A
is an abelian variety (resp. torus),
K_{\mathrm{red}}^{\circ}
is an abelian subvariety (resp. a subtorus) of
A
. Then there is an abelian subvariety (resp. a subtorus)
A^{\prime}
of
A
such that
K_{\mathrm{fin}}:=K_{\mathrm{red}}^{\circ}\cap A^{\prime}
is finite and such that
K_{\mathrm{red}}^{\circ}\cdot A^{\prime}=A
; it follows from the Poincaré reducibility theorem in the abelian variety case. The quotient
K_{2}:=K_{\mathrm{red}}^{\circ}/K_{\mathrm{fin}}
is an abelian variety (resp. a torus). Then there is a surjective homomorphism
\tilde{f}:A\to K^{\circ}_{\mathrm{red}}/K_{\mathrm{fin}}\times_{\kappa}A^{\prime}/K_{\mathrm{fin}}\to K_{2}\times_{\kappa}B
between abelian varieties (resp. tori), which is an isogeny since the dimensions of the source and the target are the same. Let
p_{2}
be the projection
p_{2}:K_{2}\times_{\kappa}B
to the second factor. Then
f=p_{2}\circ\tilde{f}
. Define
K_{1}:=\ker\tilde{f}
. Then we have the desired results.
∎

From the proof of Proposition
3.3
, we see that the cokernel sheaf of the inclusion
\mathcal{A}_{N}^{\tilde{\gamma}}\hookrightarrow\ker\iota^{\tilde{\gamma}}_{N}
is finite and the orders of its fibers are prime to
p
. Then
\mathcal{A}_{N}^{\tilde{\gamma}}\otimes{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}\cong\ker\iota^{\tilde{\gamma}}_{N}\otimes{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}
. Combining this with the discussion before Lemma
3.2
, we have

Corollary 3.5
.

The morphism
\iota^{\tilde{\gamma}}_{N}
is a prime-to-
p
isogeny and
\mathcal{A}^{\tilde{\gamma}}_{N}\otimes{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}\cong[\mathcal{A}]^{\mathcal{P}}
as fppf sheaves over
S
. Moreover,
\iota^{\tilde{\gamma}}
is the base change to
{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}
of the following
\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}^{\times}
-isogeny

\mathcal{A}^{\tilde{\gamma},\mathcal{O}_{F}}_{N}\xleftarrow{[N]}\mathcal{A}^{\tilde{\gamma},\mathcal{O}_{F}}_{N}\xrightarrow{\iota^{\tilde{\gamma}}_{N}}\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{O}_{F}},

which we still denote by
\iota^{\tilde{\gamma}}
.

Since, for any positive integer
M
such that
N|M
and
(M,p)=1
,
f^{\tilde{\gamma}}_{M}=\frac{M}{N}f^{\tilde{\gamma}}_{N}
. We then have
\mathcal{A}_{N}^{\tilde{\gamma}}\cong\mathcal{A}_{M}^{\tilde{\gamma}}/\mathcal{A}_{M}^{\tilde{\gamma}}[\frac{M}{N}]\cong\mathcal{A}_{M}^{\tilde{\gamma}}.
So it makes sense to define
\mathcal{A}^{\tilde{\gamma}}:=\mathcal{A}^{\tilde{\gamma}}_{N}
, where
N
is any prime-to-
p
positive integer such that
Nz^{\tilde{\gamma}}_{\sigma}\in\mathrm{End}_{S}(\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{O}_{F}})
.

Remark 3.6
.

We may say
\mathcal{A}^{\tilde{\gamma}}
is the twisting of
\mathcal{A}
by
\tilde{\gamma}
.
By Lemma
3.2
, for another choice
\tilde{\gamma}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{O}_{F,(p)})
,
\mathcal{A}^{\tilde{\gamma}^{\prime}}
and
\mathcal{A}^{\tilde{\gamma}}
lie in the same prime-to-
p
isogeny class, but might not be isomorphic. We will see later that this ambiguity can be eliminated by adding additional structures; see Proposition
3.11
.

3.2.
Twisting degenerating families

In this subsection, we construct twisting of degenerating families. As a preparation, materials in §
3.2.1
-§
3.2.3
are again from
[
36
, Sec. 3.1 and 3.2]
,
[
37
, Sec. 4.2]
and
[
33
, Sec. 4.4 and 4.5]
.

3.2.1.

Let
(G_{0},X_{0})
,
(G^{\ddagger},X^{\ddagger})
,
K_{0}
,
K^{\ddagger}
and
V_{\mathbb{Z}}
be defined as in §
2.1
. Fix a place
v|p
and denote
\mathcal{O}_{(v)}:=\mathcal{O}_{E_{0},(v)}
. Recall that
\mathcal{S}_{K_{0}}:=\mathcal{S}_{K_{0}}(G_{0},X_{0})
is the normalization of
\mathcal{S}_{K^{\ddagger}}
in
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{0}}:=\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{0}}(G_{0},X_{0})
via
\iota:\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{0}}\to\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{\ddagger}}\to\mathcal{S}_{K^{\ddagger}}
, which is a normal integral model of
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{0}}
; we will abusively denote by
\iota
the finite morphism
\mathcal{S}_{K_{0}}\to\mathcal{S}_{K^{\ddagger}}
induced by taking the relative normalization. Recall that
\square=\emptyset
or
\{p\}
.

Denote by
G_{0,{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}
the schematic closure of
G_{0}
in
G_{{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}^{\ddagger}:=\mathrm{Stab}_{G^{\ddagger}}(V_{{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}})
. Let
Z_{0}
be the center of
G_{0}
. Let
Z_{0,{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}
be the schematic closure of
Z_{0}
in
G_{0,{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}
. Then
Z_{0,{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}
is a flat affine group scheme of finite type over
{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}
. When
\square=\emptyset
,
G_{0,\mathbb{Z}_{(\square)}}=G_{0}
,
Z_{0,\mathbb{Z}_{(\square)}}=Z_{0}
, etc.

Let
\mathcal{A}
be the pullback to
\mathcal{S}_{K_{0}}
of the universal abelian scheme over
\mathcal{S}_{K^{\ddagger}}
. By
[
33
, Lem. 4.5.2]
, there is a natural embedding

\mathbf{z}:Z_{0,{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}\hookrightarrow\underline{\mathrm{Aut}}_{{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}(\mathcal{A});

on the other hand, there is a natural embedding
Z_{0,{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}\hookrightarrow G_{0,{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{GL}(V_{{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}})
by the construction. Then there are natural
Z_{0,\mathbb{Z}_{(\square)}}
-actions on both
V^{\square}\mathcal{A}
and
V_{\mathbb{A}_{f}^{\square}}
induced by the two embeddings above.

We denote by
(\mathcal{A},\lambda,[\varepsilon_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{\square}}]_{K^{{\ddagger},\square}})
the pullback to
\mathcal{S}_{K_{0}}
(resp.
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{0}}
) of the universal family over
\mathcal{S}_{K^{\ddagger}}
(resp.
\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{\ddagger}}
) obtained from the moduli problem
\mathbf{M}^{\mathrm{iso}}_{(V_{\mathbb{Z}},\psi_{\mathbb{Z}}),K^{{\ddagger},\square}}
when
\square=\{p\}
(resp.
\emptyset
). Let
[\varepsilon_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{\square}}]_{K^{\square}_{0}}:=\varprojlim_{K_{0}^{\square}\subset K^{{\ddagger},\square}}[\varepsilon_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{\square}}]_{K^{{\ddagger},\square}}
.

Lemma 3.7

(
[
37
, Lem. 4.2.4]
)
.

With the conventions above, fix any representative
\varepsilon_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{p}}
in
[\varepsilon_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{p}}]_{K^{p}_{0}}
.
Let
R
be a
{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}
-algebra contained in
\mathbb{C}
. Then the isomorphism

\varepsilon_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{p}}:V_{\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p}}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}R\longrightarrow V^{p}\mathcal{A}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}R

is
Z_{0,{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}(R)
-equivariant.

3.2.2.

We equip
V_{{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}
with the action of
Z_{0,{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}
induced by the embedding
Z_{0,{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}\hookrightarrow G_{0,{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{GL}(V_{{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}})
.

We denote by
\mathbf{V}_{{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}
the
{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}
-module
V_{{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}
equipped with the trivial
Z_{0,{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}
-action. Let
\mathbf{V}_{\mathbb{Z}}:=\mathbf{V}_{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}\cap V_{\mathbb{Z}}
.
We denote by
\mathbf{V}:=\mathbf{V}_{{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}\mathbb{Q}
(resp.
V:=V_{{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}\mathbb{Q}
) the vector space
V
with
Z_{0}
-action induced by the
Z_{0,{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}
-action on
\mathbf{V}_{{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}
(resp.
V_{{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}
).

Let
G^{\mathrm{ad}}_{0,{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}:=G_{0,{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}/Z_{0,{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}
. Let
G_{0,{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}^{\mathrm{ad}}({\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}})_{1}
be the subgroup of
G_{0,{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}^{\mathrm{ad}}(\mathbb{Q})=G^{\mathrm{ad}}_{0}(\mathbb{Q})
that stabilizes
X_{0}\hookrightarrow X_{0}^{\mathrm{ad}}
, that is,
G_{0,{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}^{\mathrm{ad}}({\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}})_{1}:=G_{0,{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}^{\mathrm{ad}}({\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}})\cap G^{\mathrm{ad}}(\mathbb{R})_{1}
. Let
\gamma\in G_{0,{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}^{\mathrm{ad}}({\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}})_{1}
and let
\mathcal{P}
be the fiber of
G_{0,{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}\to G^{\mathrm{ad}}_{0,{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}
at
\gamma
.
As in §
3.1.4
, there is a finite Galois extension
F
over
\mathbb{Q}
such that
\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{O}_{F,(p)})
is non-empty.

Let
\tilde{\gamma}
be any element in
\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{O}_{F,(p)})
lifting
\gamma
. Then the
Z_{0,{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}
-action on
\mathbf{V}_{{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}
(resp.
V_{{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}
) specializes to a
\mathcal{O}_{F,(p)}
-semilinear
\mathrm{Gal}(F/\mathbb{Q})
-action on
\mathbf{V}_{{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}\mathcal{O}_{F,(p)}
(resp.
V_{{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}\mathcal{O}_{F,(p)}
).

With the conventions above, the isomorphism between
\mathcal{O}_{F,(p)}
-modules
(\tilde{\gamma}^{-1}\cdot):\mathbf{V}_{{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}\mathcal{O}_{F,(p)}\to V_{{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{F,(p)}
formed by left-multiplying
\tilde{\gamma}^{-1}
on the underlying
\mathcal{O}_{F,(p)}
-modules is
\mathrm{Gal}(F/\mathbb{Q})
-equivariant: Indeed,
z_{\sigma}^{\tilde{\gamma}}(1\otimes\sigma)\tilde{\gamma}^{-1}=(z_{\sigma}^{\tilde{\gamma}}z_{\sigma}^{\tilde{\gamma}^{-1}}\tilde{\gamma}^{-1})(1\otimes\sigma)=\tilde{\gamma}^{-1}(1\otimes\sigma)
since
\tilde{\gamma}{\tilde{\gamma}^{-1}}=1\in G_{0,{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}(\mathcal{O}_{F,(p)})
.
Together with Lemma
3.7
, we obtain that

Proposition 3.8
.

The composition

(3.5)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbf{V}_{\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p}}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}\mathcal{O}_{F,(p)}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{V_{\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p}}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}\mathcal{O}_{F,(p)}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V^{p}\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{O}_{F}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces({\tilde{\gamma}}^{-1}\cdot)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varepsilon_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{p}}\otimes 1}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}

is
\mathrm{Gal}(F/\mathbb{Q})
-equivariant.

Then
{}^{\gamma}{K^{p}_{0}}:=\gamma K^{p}_{0}\gamma^{-1}
(see the convention list after the introduction) acts on
(\varepsilon_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{p}}\otimes 1)\circ(\tilde{\gamma}^{-1}\cdot)
by right-composition, i.e.,
\gamma g\gamma^{-1}\in{}^{\gamma}{K_{0}^{p}}
sends

(\varepsilon_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{p}}\otimes 1)\circ(\tilde{\gamma}^{-1}\cdot)\mapsto(\varepsilon_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{p}}\otimes 1)\circ(\tilde{\gamma}^{-1}\cdot)\circ\gamma g\gamma^{-1}.

Then

(\varepsilon_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{p}}\otimes 1)\circ{\tilde{\gamma}}^{-1}(\mathbf{V}_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{p}}\otimes F)
is
\mathrm{Gal}(F/\mathbb{Q})
-stable, and the
K_{0}^{p}
-orbit of it is
\pi_{1}(S,\overline{s})
-invariant when specializing to any geometric point
\overline{s}
of
S
.

Assume now that
{}^{\gamma}{K_{0}^{p}}
stabilizes
\mathbf{V}_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{p}}
. Consider the image of
\mathbf{V}_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{p}}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{F}\subset\mathbf{V}_{\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p}}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}\mathcal{O}_{F,(p)}
in
V^{p}\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{O}_{F}}
via
(\varepsilon_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{p}}\otimes 1)\circ{\tilde{\gamma}}^{-1}
.
Then there is a
\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}^{\times}
-isogeny

f:\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{O}_{F}}\longrightarrow\mathcal{A}^{\prime}

over
S
which induces an isomorphism
V^{p}f:V^{p}\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{O}_{F}}\xrightarrow{\sim}V^{p}\mathcal{A}^{\prime}
such that
V^{p}f\circ(\varepsilon_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{p}}\otimes 1)\circ{\tilde{\gamma}}^{-1}(\mathbf{V}_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{p}}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{F})=T^{p}\mathcal{A}^{\prime}
; the sheaf
[\mathcal{A}^{\prime}]
is equipped with a
\mathrm{Gal}(F/\mathbb{Q})
-action induced by
f
.

By Proposition
3.8
, we can take the
\mathrm{Gal}(F/\mathbb{Q})
-invariant part so that there is an isomorphism

\varepsilon_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{p}}^{\tilde{\gamma}}:\mathbf{V}_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{p}}\longrightarrow(T^{p}\mathcal{A}^{\prime})^{\mathrm{Gal}(F/\mathbb{Q})}.

The open compact subgroup
V^{p}f\circ(\varepsilon_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{p},\overline{s}}\otimes 1)\circ{\tilde{\gamma}}^{-1}(\mathbf{V}_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{p}}\otimes 1)
of
V^{p}\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{O}_{F}}_{\overline{s}}
is
\mathrm{Gal}(F/\mathbb{Q})
-invariant and
\pi_{1}(S,\overline{s})
-invariant (by the assumption on
{}^{\gamma}{K_{0}^{p}}
), so it is contained in
V^{p}\mathcal{A}_{\overline{s}}^{\tilde{\gamma}}
, where

\mathcal{A}^{\tilde{\gamma}}
is the twisted abelian scheme constructed in §
3.1
. Then, from the construction above, there is a
\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}^{\times}
-isogeny
f^{\tilde{\gamma}}:\mathcal{A}^{\tilde{\gamma}}\to\mathcal{A}^{\tilde{\gamma},\prime}
such that
T^{p}\mathcal{A}^{\tilde{\gamma},\prime}=(T^{p}\mathcal{A}^{\prime})^{\mathrm{Gal}(F/\mathbb{Q})}
.
Hence, we write
\varepsilon^{\tilde{\gamma}}_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{p}}
as

\varepsilon^{\tilde{\gamma}}_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{p}}:\mathbf{V}_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{p}}\longrightarrow T^{p}\mathcal{A}^{\tilde{\gamma},\prime}.

Lemma 3.9
.

The isomorphism
\varepsilon^{\tilde{\gamma}}_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{p}}
is independent of the choice of
\tilde{\gamma}\in\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{O}_{F,(p)})
lifting
\gamma
.

Proof.

Let
\widetilde{\gamma}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{O}_{F,(p)})
such that
{\tilde{\gamma}}^{\prime}=z\tilde{\gamma}
for some
z\in Z_{0,{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}(\mathcal{O}_{F,(p)})
. There is a commutative diagram

(3.6)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{V}_{\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p}}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}\mathcal{O}_{F,(p)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V_{\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p}}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}\mathcal{O}_{F,(p)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V^{p}\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{O}_{F}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V^{p}\mathcal{A}^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V_{\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p}}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}\mathcal{O}_{F,(p)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V^{p}\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{O}_{F}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces({\tilde{\gamma}}^{-1}\cdot)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces({\tilde{\gamma}}^{\prime,-1}\cdot)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces z^{-1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varepsilon_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{p}}\otimes 1}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces z^{-1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V^{p}f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varepsilon_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{p}}\otimes 1}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V^{p}f^{\prime}}

The composition
(\varepsilon_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{p}}\otimes 1)\circ({\tilde{\gamma}}^{-1}\cdot)
(resp.
(\varepsilon_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{p}}\otimes 1)\circ({\tilde{\gamma}}^{\prime,-1}\cdot)
) induces a
\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}^{\times}
-isogeny
f:\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{O}_{F}}\to\mathcal{A}^{\prime}
(resp.
f^{\prime}:\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{O}_{F}}\to\mathcal{A}^{\prime}
), such that
f=f^{\prime}\circ z^{-1}
, since the images of
\mathbf{V}_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{p}}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{F}
in
V^{p}\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{O}_{F}}
via
z^{-1}\circ(\varepsilon_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{p}}\otimes 1)\circ({\tilde{\gamma}}^{-1}\cdot)
and
(\varepsilon_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{p}}\otimes 1)\circ({\tilde{\gamma}}^{\prime,-1}\cdot)
coincide with each other. Then we have a commutative diagram above by
[
44
, Cor. 1.3.5.4]
and Lemma
3.7
, and we have the desired claim by taking the
\mathrm{Gal}(F/\mathbb{Q})
-invariant part.
∎

Denote
\varepsilon_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{p}}^{\tilde{\gamma}}
as
\varepsilon_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{p}}^{\gamma}
. We then call the
{}^{\gamma}{K_{0}^{p}}
-orbit
[\varepsilon_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{p}}^{\gamma}]_{{}^{\gamma}{K_{0}^{p}}}
a
twisted (integral) level structure
.

3.2.3.

Recall that
\nu:G^{\ddagger}\to\mathbb{G}_{m}
denotes the similitude character. Denote by
\psi^{\mathbf{V}}_{\mathbb{Z}}:\mathbf{V}_{\mathbb{Z}}\times\mathbf{V}_{\mathbb{Z}}\to\mathbb{Z}(1)
the pairing
\psi_{\mathbb{Z}}
viewed as a pairing for
\mathbf{V}_{\mathbb{Z}}
. We can also obtain a pairing

\psi_{\mathbb{Z}}\otimes 1:(V_{\mathbb{Z}}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{F,(p)})\times(V_{\mathbb{Z}}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{F,(p)})\to\mathcal{O}_{F,(p)}(\nu)

by the base change of
\psi_{\mathbb{Z}}
to
\mathcal{O}_{F,(p)}
.
Similarly, define

\psi^{\mathbf{V}}_{\mathbb{Z}}\otimes 1:(\mathbf{V}_{\mathbb{Z}}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{F,(p)})\times(\mathbf{V}_{\mathbb{Z}}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{F,(p)})\to\mathcal{O}_{F,(p)}(\nu).

Note that
\mathrm{Gal}(F/\mathbb{Q})
acts on
\mathcal{O}_{F,(p)}(\nu)
via
\sigma\mapsto\nu(z^{\tilde{\gamma}}_{\sigma})\sigma
for
\psi_{\mathbb{Z}}\otimes 1
and via
\sigma
for
\psi^{\mathbf{V}}_{\mathbb{Z}}\otimes 1
.
Hence, there is a
\mathrm{Gal}(F/\mathbb{Q})
-equivariant map

\tilde{\gamma}^{-1}:(\mathbf{V}\otimes{{\mathcal{O}_{F}}}\otimes{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}},\psi^{\mathbf{V}}_{\mathbb{Z}}\otimes 1)\to(V\otimes{{\mathcal{O}_{F}}}\otimes{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}},\nu(\tilde{\gamma})\circ\psi_{\mathbb{Z}}\otimes 1).

On the other hand, we do the same procedures for
\mathcal{A}^{\vee}
as above. First, we obtain an abelian scheme
\mathcal{A}^{\vee,\tilde{\gamma}}
, which is defined by the
Z_{0,{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}
-action given by the canonical isomorphism (
B.2
).

We then consider the diagram below.

(3.7)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbf{V}_{\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p}}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}\mathcal{O}_{F,(p)}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V_{\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p}}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}\mathcal{O}_{F,(p)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V^{p}\mathcal{A}^{{\mathcal{O}_{F}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V^{p}\mathcal{A}^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbf{V}_{\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p}}^{\vee}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}\mathcal{O}_{F,(p)}(\nu)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{V_{\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p}}^{\vee}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}\mathcal{O}_{F,(p)}(\nu)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V^{p}\mathcal{A}^{\vee}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}\mathcal{O}_{F,(p)}(\nu)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V^{p}\mathcal{A}^{*}(\nu)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\tilde{\gamma}^{-1}\cdot)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\psi^{\mathbf{V}}\otimes 1}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varepsilon_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{p}}\otimes 1}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\psi\otimes 1}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V^{p}f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V^{p}\lambda\otimes 1}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V^{p}\lambda^{\mathbf{V},{\mathcal{O}_{F}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V^{p}f^{\prime}}

The first two vertical maps are canonically induced by the perfect pairings marked on the arrows.
Let us explain how to produce the arrows in the lower-right corner.
In fact, let
\mathcal{U}^{\vee}
be the image of
\mathbf{V}_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{p}}^{\vee}\otimes{\mathcal{O}_{F}}
in
V^{p}\mathcal{A}^{\vee}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}\mathcal{O}_{F,(p)}(\nu)
via the composition
(V^{p}\lambda\otimes 1)\circ(\varepsilon_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{p}}\otimes 1)\circ(\tilde{\gamma}^{-1}\cdot)\circ(\psi^{\mathbf{V}}\otimes 1)^{-1}
. Then, by
[
44
, Cor. 1.3.5.4]
(see also
[
61
, Lem. 4.22]
),
\mathcal{U}^{\vee}
determines a
\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}^{\times}
-isogeny
f^{\prime}:(\mathcal{A}^{\vee})^{{\mathcal{O}_{F}}}\to\mathcal{A}^{*}
and a prime-to-
p
isogeny
\lambda^{\mathbf{V},{\mathcal{O}_{F}}}:\mathcal{A}^{\prime}\to\mathcal{A}^{*}
so that the right square of the diagram above commutes.

By Lemma
3.7
,
\varepsilon_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{p}}\otimes 1
is
Z_{0,{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}(\mathcal{O}_{F,(p)})
-equivariant. Combining this with the facts that
\varepsilon_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{p}}\otimes 1
sends
\psi_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{p}}
to a
\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{p,\times}
-multiple of
\mathrm{e}^{\lambda}
, and that
\psi_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{p}}
is
Z_{0,{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}(\mathcal{O}_{F,(p)})
-equivariant, we obtain that
V^{p}\lambda\otimes 1
is
Z_{0,{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}(\mathcal{O}_{F,(p)})
-equivariant and therefore is
\mathrm{Gal}(F/\mathbb{Q})
-equivariant. Hence, this is also true for
V^{p}f^{\prime}
and
V^{p}\lambda^{\mathbf{V},{\mathcal{O}_{F}}}
.

Moreover, since

V^{p}(\mathcal{A}^{\vee})^{\tilde{\gamma}}=(\lambda^{\mathbf{V},{\mathcal{O}_{F}}}\circ V^{p}f\circ(\varepsilon_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{p}}\otimes 1)\circ({\tilde{\gamma}}^{-1}\cdot)\circ(\psi^{\mathbf{V}}\otimes 1)^{-1})(\mathbf{V}^{\vee}_{\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p}}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}\mathcal{O}_{F,(p)}(\nu))^{\mathrm{Gal}(F/\mathbb{Q})},

the image of
\mathbf{V}^{\vee}_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{p}}
in
V^{p}(\mathcal{A}^{\vee})^{\tilde{\gamma}}
induces a
\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}^{\times}
-isogeny
(\mathcal{A}^{\vee})^{\tilde{\gamma}}\to\mathcal{A}^{\prime\prime}
such that
T^{p}\mathcal{A}^{\prime\prime}=(T^{p}\mathcal{A}^{*})^{\mathrm{Gal}(F/\mathbb{Q})}
. There is also a prime-to-
p
isogeny
\lambda^{\tilde{\gamma},\prime}:\mathcal{A}^{\tilde{\gamma},\prime}\to\mathcal{A}^{\prime\prime}
which is the
\mathrm{Gal}(F/\mathbb{Q})
-invariant of
\lambda^{\mathbf{V},{\mathcal{O}_{F}}}:\mathcal{A}^{\prime}\to\mathcal{A}^{*}
.

Lemma 3.10
.

With the conventions above, the abelian scheme
\mathcal{A}^{\prime\prime}
is isomorphic to the dual of
\mathcal{A}^{\tilde{\gamma},\prime}
, and the isogeny

\lambda^{\tilde{\gamma},\prime}:\mathcal{A}^{\tilde{\gamma},\prime}\to\mathcal{A}^{\prime\prime}

is a polarization.

Proof.

Choose any positive definite perfect pairing
\mathrm{e}_{0}:{\mathcal{O}_{F}}\times{\mathcal{O}_{F}}\to\mathbb{Z}
which canonically induces an isomorphism of
\mathbb{Z}
-modules
i_{F}:{\mathcal{O}_{F}}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathbb{Z}}({\mathcal{O}_{F}},\mathbb{Z})
. Since
\lambda(i_{F}):\mathcal{A}^{{\mathcal{O}_{F}}}\xrightarrow{\lambda}\mathcal{A}^{\vee}\otimes{\mathcal{O}_{F}}\xrightarrow{i_{F}}\mathcal{A}^{\vee}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{F}^{\vee}
is a polarization (see
[
1
]
), so is
\lambda^{\mathbf{V},{\mathcal{O}_{F}}}(i_{F}):\mathcal{A}^{\prime}\xrightarrow{\lambda^{\mathbf{V},{\mathcal{O}_{F}}}}\mathcal{A}^{*}\xrightarrow{i_{F}}\mathcal{A}^{*}\otimes_{{\mathcal{O}_{F}},i_{F}}\mathcal{O}_{F}^{\vee}
. Hence, we have obtained a polarization

\mathcal{A}^{\tilde{\gamma},\prime}\otimes{\mathcal{O}_{F}}\xrightarrow{\lambda^{\tilde{\gamma},\prime}\otimes 1}\mathcal{A}^{\prime\prime}\otimes{\mathcal{O}_{F}}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathcal{A}^{\prime\prime}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{F}^{\vee}.

Taking the
\mathrm{Gal}(F/\mathbb{Q})
-invariant part, we obtain a polarization
\mathcal{A}^{\tilde{\gamma},\prime}\otimes\mathbb{Z}\xrightarrow{\lambda^{\tilde{\gamma},\prime}\otimes 1}\mathcal{A}^{\prime\prime}\otimes\mathbb{Z}\xrightarrow[\sim]{i_{F}}\mathcal{A}^{\prime\prime}\otimes\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathbb{Z}}(\mathbb{Z},\mathbb{Z})
(where the second isomorphism is induced by the unique positive definite perfect duality
\mathbb{Z}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathbb{Z}}(\mathbb{Z},\mathbb{Z})
and does not depend on
i_{F}
), which implies that
\lambda^{\tilde{\gamma},\prime}
is also a polarization.
∎

Let
\mathcal{A}^{\gamma}:=\mathcal{A}^{\tilde{\gamma},\prime}
and
\lambda^{\gamma}:=\lambda^{\tilde{\gamma},\prime}
.

Proposition 3.11
.

Let
\gamma\in G_{0,{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}^{\mathrm{ad}}({\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}})_{1}
. For the tuple
(\mathcal{A},\lambda,[\varepsilon_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{p}}]_{K^{p}_{0}})
, we can associate with it a tuple
(\mathcal{A}^{\gamma},\lambda^{\gamma},[\varepsilon_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{p}}^{\gamma}]_{{}^{\gamma}{K^{p}_{0}}})
, which is independent of the choice of
\tilde{\gamma}
lifting
\gamma
. For any open compact subgroup
K^{{\ddagger},p,\prime}
of
G^{\ddagger}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
containing
{}^{\gamma}{K^{p}}
that stabilizes
\mathbf{V}_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{p}}
, the tuple
(\mathcal{A}^{\gamma},\lambda^{\gamma},[\varepsilon_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{p}}^{\gamma}]_{K^{{\ddagger},p,\prime}})
is an object in
\mathbf{M}_{(\mathbf{V}_{\mathbb{Z}},\psi^{\mathbf{V}}_{\mathbb{Z}}),K^{{\ddagger},p,\prime}}^{\mathrm{iso}}
.

This association defines a morphism
\gamma^{-1}:\mathcal{S}_{K}\to\mathcal{S}_{{}^{\gamma}{K}}
, whose generic fiber
\gamma^{-1}:\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{0}}\to\mathrm{Sh}_{{}^{\gamma}{K_{0}}}
is determined by the map
[(x,g)]\mapsto[(\gamma(x),\gamma g\gamma^{-1})]
over complex points.

Proof.

The first paragraph is from the main construction in §
3.2.2
and the paragraphs above. The tuple
(\mathcal{A}^{\gamma},\lambda^{\gamma},[\varepsilon^{\gamma}_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{p}}]_{K^{{\ddagger},p,\prime}})
viewed as an object in
\mathbf{M}^{\mathrm{isog}}_{K^{{\ddagger},p,\prime}}
is exactly the twisting construction in
[
33
, Sec. 4.5]
. Combining it with
[
36
, Lem. 3.2.6]
, we obtain the second paragraph as in
[
33
, Lem. 4.5.7]
by normalization.
∎

3.2.4.

Let
\Sigma_{0}
be any admissible polyhedral cone decomposition for
\mathcal{S}_{K_{0}}
that is compatible with an admissible polyhedral cone decomposition
\Sigma^{\ddagger}
for
\mathcal{S}_{K^{\ddagger}}
. Let
\mathcal{S}^{\Sigma_{0}}_{K_{0}}
(resp.
\mathcal{S}^{\Sigma^{\ddagger}}_{K^{\ddagger}}
) be the toroidal compactification of
\mathcal{S}_{K_{0}}
(resp.
\mathcal{S}_{K^{\ddagger}}
) associated with
\Sigma_{0}
(resp.
\Sigma^{\ddagger}
). Let
S:=\mathcal{S}^{\Sigma_{0}}_{K_{0}}
and
U:=\mathcal{S}_{K_{0}}
.

For any
K^{p}_{0}
contained in
K^{{\ddagger},p}
as in §
2.1
, we denote by
(\mathcal{G},\lambda_{S},[\varepsilon_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{p}}]_{K^{{\ddagger},p}})
the pullback of the degenerating family over
\mathcal{S}_{K^{\ddagger}}^{\Sigma^{\ddagger}}
to
S
. Then
(\mathcal{G},\lambda_{S},[\varepsilon_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{p}}]_{K^{{\ddagger},p}})
is a degenerating family in
\mathbf{DEG}_{(V_{\mathbb{Z}},\psi_{\mathbb{Z}}),K^{{\ddagger},p}}(S,U)
extending
(\mathcal{A},\lambda,[\varepsilon_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{p}}]_{K^{{\ddagger},p}})
. From §
3.2.1
, we obtain a tuple
(\mathcal{G},\lambda_{S},[\varepsilon_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{p}}]_{K^{p}_{0}})
.

Denote
[\mathcal{G}]:=\mathcal{G}\otimes{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}
, which represents
\mathcal{G}
up to prime-to-
p
isogenies.

Lemma 3.12
.

There is a natural isomorphism
\underline{\mathrm{Aut}}_{{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}([\mathcal{G}])\cong\underline{\mathrm{Aut}}_{{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}([\mathcal{A}])
induced by restricting automorphisms of
[\mathcal{G}]
from
S
to
U
. In particular,
\mathbf{z}:Z_{0,{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}\to\underline{\mathrm{Aut}}_{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}([\mathcal{A}])
extends uniquely to a homomorphism

\mathbf{z}^{\mathcal{G}}:Z_{0,{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}\longrightarrow\underline{\mathrm{Aut}}_{{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}([\mathcal{G}]).

Proof.

The second statement follows from the first. By
[
21
, I. Prop. 2.7]
, any homomorphism
f_{U}\in\mathrm{End}_{U}(\mathcal{A})
extends uniquely to a homomorphism
f_{S}\in\mathrm{End}_{S}(\mathcal{G})
. Moreover,
f_{S}
is a prime-to-
p
isogeny if and only if
f_{U}
is so.
(Indeed, if
f_{U}\circ g_{U}=g_{U}\circ f_{U}=[M]
for some
g_{U}\in\mathrm{End}_{U}(\mathcal{A})
and
(M,p)
=1, then
g_{U}
extends uniquely to
g_{S}\in\mathrm{End}_{S}(\mathcal{G})
such that
f_{S}\circ g_{S}=g_{S}\circ f_{S}=[M]
, so the claim is true since
[M]
is a prime-to-
p
isogeny for
\mathcal{G}
.) Hence,
\mathrm{End}_{S}(\mathcal{G})\cong\mathrm{End}_{U}(\mathcal{A})
, which induces an isomorphism
\mathrm{End}_{S}([\mathcal{G}])\cong\mathrm{End}_{U}([\mathcal{A}])
. So
(\mathrm{End}_{S}([\mathcal{G}])\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}R)^{\times}\cong(\mathrm{End}_{U}([\mathcal{A}])\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}R)^{\times}
, as desired.
∎

For any
\gamma\in G_{0,{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}^{\mathrm{ad}}({\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}})_{1}
as in §
3.2.2
, we describe the twisting of
(\mathcal{G},\lambda,[\varepsilon_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{p}}])
by
\gamma
. Let
F
and
\tilde{\gamma}
be as in §
3.1
. Denote by
f^{\tilde{\gamma}}_{N}
the homomorphism defining
\mathcal{A}^{\tilde{\gamma}}
as in §
3.1
. Then Lemma
3.12
implies that
f^{\tilde{\gamma}}_{N}
extends uniquely to a homomorphism

f^{\tilde{\gamma}}_{N,S}:\mathcal{G}^{\mathcal{O}_{F}}\longrightarrow(\mathcal{G}^{\mathcal{O}_{F}})^{\oplus|\mathrm{Gal}(F/\mathbb{Q})|-1}

over
S
, which can still be written as
f^{\tilde{\gamma}}_{N,S}=\bigoplus\limits_{\sigma\in\mathrm{Gal}(F/\mathbb{Q})\text{ and }\sigma\neq\mathrm{id}}((Nz_{\sigma}^{\tilde{\gamma}})\sigma-N\cdot\mathrm{id})
. Denote by
(\ker f^{\tilde{\gamma}}_{N,S})^{\circ}
the identity component of the kernel of
f^{\tilde{\gamma}}_{N,S}
.

Lemma 3.13
.

(\ker f^{\tilde{\gamma}}_{N,S})^{\circ}
is a semi-abelian scheme over
S
.

Proof.

Arguing as in the first paragraph of the proof of Proposition
3.3
, we only need to show that the fiber
(\ker f^{\tilde{\gamma}}_{N,S})^{\circ}_{t}
at
t
is an extension of an abelian variety by a torus for
t\in S\backslash U
.

Write
\mathcal{G}_{t}
as an extension
0\to T_{t}\to\mathcal{G}_{t}\to\mathcal{A}_{t}\to 0.
Let
f
be any
surjective
homomorphism between
\mathcal{G}^{\mathcal{O}_{F}}_{t}
. Then
f
maps
T_{t}^{\mathcal{O}_{F}}
to itself and also induces an endomorphism of
\mathcal{A}_{t}^{\mathcal{O}_{F}}
by taking the quotient of
\mathcal{G}_{t}^{{\mathcal{O}_{F}}}
by
T_{t}^{{\mathcal{O}_{F}}}
: To see this, note that
\mathcal{G}_{t}^{\mathcal{O}_{F}}/f(T_{t}^{\mathcal{O}_{F}})
only has abelian part since it is the image of the proper scheme
\mathcal{A}_{t}^{\mathcal{O}_{F}}
.

By applying the last paragraph to
Nz_{\sigma}^{\tilde{\gamma}}\sigma
and
N\cdot\mathrm{id}
, we see that
f^{\tilde{\gamma}}_{N,t}
maps the torus part of
\mathcal{G}_{t}^{\mathcal{O}_{F}}
to the torus part of its target, and induces a homomorphism
f^{\tilde{\gamma},ab}_{N,t}
from the abelian part of
\mathcal{G}_{t}^{\mathcal{O}_{F}}
to the abelian part of its target. By Proposition
3.3
,
(\ker f^{\tilde{\gamma},ab}_{N,t})^{\circ}
is an abelian variety, and
(\ker f^{\tilde{\gamma}}_{N,t}|_{T_{t}^{\mathcal{O}_{F}}})^{\circ}
is a torus.
Finally, we note that there is an exact sequence

0\longrightarrow\ker f^{\tilde{\gamma}}_{N,t}|_{T_{t}^{\mathcal{O}_{F}}}\longrightarrow\ker f^{\tilde{\gamma}}_{N,t}\longrightarrow\ker f^{\tilde{\gamma},ab}_{N,t}\longrightarrow\Lambda

with
\Lambda
some finite algebraic group. Indeed,
\ker f^{\tilde{\gamma},ab}_{N,t}
is proper and
\operatorname{coker}f^{\tilde{\gamma}}_{N,t}|_{T_{t}^{\mathcal{O}_{F}}}
is an affine group, so it follows by snake lemma. So the homomorphism
(\ker f^{\tilde{\gamma}}_{N,t})^{\circ}\to(\ker f^{\tilde{\gamma},ab}_{N,t})^{\circ}
must be surjective:
(\ker f^{\tilde{\gamma},ab}_{N,t})^{\circ}
is covered by its fiber in
\ker f^{\tilde{\gamma}}_{N,t}
, which is a union of connected components of
\ker f^{\tilde{\gamma}}_{N,t}
. Then the images of any two connected components of the fiber are equal.

After taking the identity component of
\ker f^{\tilde{\gamma}}_{N,t}
, we have an exact sequence

1\longrightarrow\widetilde{T}\longrightarrow(\ker f^{\tilde{\gamma}}_{N,t})^{\circ}\longrightarrow\mathcal{A}_{t}^{\tilde{\gamma}}\longrightarrow 1,

where the first term
\widetilde{T}
is an algebraic group of multiplicative type. Then we see that the quotient
(\ker f^{\tilde{\gamma}}_{N,t})^{\circ}/\widetilde{T}^{\circ}
is a connected commutative group variety which is an étale cover of
\mathcal{A}^{\tilde{\gamma}}_{t}
, and therefore is also an abelian variety. Then we have proved the desired result.
∎

We denote
\mathcal{G}^{\tilde{\gamma}}:=(\ker f^{\tilde{\gamma}}_{N,S})^{\circ}
.

Proposition 3.14
.

With the conventions and constructions above,
(\mathcal{A}^{\gamma},\lambda^{\gamma})
extends uniquely to a pair
(\mathcal{G}^{\gamma},\lambda^{\gamma}_{S})
with
\mathcal{G}^{\gamma}
a semi-abelian scheme over
S
and
\lambda^{\gamma}_{S}
an isogeny
\lambda^{\gamma}_{S}:\mathcal{G}^{\gamma}\to\mathcal{G}^{\gamma,\vee}
.

Proof.

By
[
21
, I. Prop. 2.7]
,
\lambda^{\tilde{\gamma}}
extends uniquely to an isogeny
\lambda_{S}^{\gamma}
if
\mathcal{A}^{\gamma}
and
\mathcal{A}^{\gamma,\vee}
extend to
\mathcal{G}^{\gamma}
and
\mathcal{G}^{\gamma,\vee}
; the uniqueness of such
\mathcal{G}^{\gamma}
and
\mathcal{G}^{\gamma,\vee}
also follows from
loc. cit
.

There is a natural
\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}^{\times}
-isogeny
f^{\tilde{\gamma}}:(\mathcal{A}^{\tilde{\gamma}},\lambda^{\tilde{\gamma}})\to(\mathcal{A}^{\gamma},\lambda^{\gamma})
by Proposition
3.11
. Choose any prime-to-
p
integer
M>0
such that
[M]\circ f^{\tilde{\gamma}}
is a prime-to-
p
isogeny. Denote by
d
the degree of
[M]\circ f^{\tilde{\gamma}}
. Then
\ker[M]\circ f^{\tilde{\gamma}}\subset\mathcal{A}^{\tilde{\gamma}}[d]
.
Denote by
\overline{K}
the closure of
\ker[M]\circ f^{\tilde{\gamma}}
in
\mathcal{G}^{\tilde{\gamma}}[d]
. By the following general fact Lemma
3.15
,
\overline{K}
is quasi-finite and étale. Then the quotient
\mathcal{G}^{\gamma}:=\mathcal{G}^{\tilde{\gamma}}/\overline{K}
is a semi-abelian scheme extending
\mathcal{A}^{\gamma}
by
[
56
, IV, 7.1.2]
(see also
[
44
, Lem. 3.4.3.1]
).
∎

Lemma 3.15
.

Let
X
be a quasi-finite separated commutative group scheme over a locally Noetherian normal scheme
S
over
{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}
. Suppose that the order of
X
over every fiber is prime-to-
p
, and that there is a dense open subscheme
U
of
S
over which
X
is quasi-finite and étale. Then the schematic closure
\overline{X}
of
X_{U}
in
X
is étale over
S
.

Proof.

We can show this by passing to the strict Henselization
\mathcal{O}_{t}^{sh}:=\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{S,t}^{sh}
of the complete local ring
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{S,t}
, for any
t\in S\backslash U
.
Moreover, we can replace
U
with the étale locus of
\overline{X}
over
S
. If the lemma were not true, the complement
D:=S\backslash U
would be closed and
nonempty
.
We can further let
t
be a minimal prime ideal in
D
. Denote the special fiber of
\mathcal{O}_{t}^{sh}
by
\overline{t}
and the complement of
\overline{t}
in
\mathcal{O}_{t}^{sh}
by
U_{t}
. To find the contradiction, we show the claim that
\overline{X}_{\mathcal{O}^{sh}_{t}}
is étale over
\mathcal{O}_{t}^{sh}
.

As
\overline{X}
is quasi-finite and separated, it can be decomposed as a disjoint union of schemes
\overline{X}_{\mathcal{O}^{sh}_{t}}=\overline{X}^{f}\coprod\overline{X}^{\emptyset}
, where
\overline{X}^{f}
is a finite, open, and closed subgroup of
\overline{X}_{\mathcal{O}^{sh}_{t}}
and
\overline{X}^{\emptyset}_{\overline{t}}=\emptyset
(see, e.g.,
[
44
, 3.4.1]
). Over
U_{t}
,
\overline{X}_{U_{t}}=X_{U_{t}}
is étale (and therefore quasi-finite étale over
\mathcal{O}_{t}^{sh}
).
So it suffices to show the claim for
\overline{X}^{f}
. Since the commutative group scheme
\overline{X}^{f}_{\overline{t}}
is contained in
X_{\overline{t}}
, it is of prime-to-
p
order, so each of its geometrically connected components must be trivial. Hence,
\overline{X}^{f}_{\overline{t}}\cong\pi_{0}(\overline{X}^{f}_{\overline{t}})
is finite étale over
\overline{t}
. By
[
22
, 18.8.3, d)]
, for any point
x_{0}\in\overline{X}^{f}_{\overline{t}}
, there is an affine open neighborhood
U_{x_{0}}
of
x_{0}
in
\overline{X}^{f}
such that the restriction to
U_{x_{0}}
of the structure morphism to
\spec\mathcal{O}_{t}^{sh}
is a closed embedding. Hence, we can write
U_{t}
as a disjoint union of open and closed subschemes
U_{1}
and
U_{2}
such that
U_{1}
is the intersection of
U_{t}
and the image of
U_{x_{0}}
under the structure morphism. However, the closures of
U_{1}
and
U_{2}
in
\mathcal{O}^{sh}_{t}
are two closed subschemes. Since
\mathcal{O}_{S,t}
is a normal domain, so is
\mathcal{O}_{t}^{sh}
. So either
U_{1}
or
U_{2}
is empty. But the former case would imply that
x_{0}
is not in the closure
\overline{X}_{\mathcal{O}^{sh}_{t}}
. So
U_{2}
is empty, and therefore the morphism of
U_{x_{0}}
to
\spec\mathcal{O}_{t}^{sh}
is a surjective closed immersion. So the claim is true and the lemma is proved.
∎

Let
[\varepsilon^{\gamma}_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{p}}]_{{}^{\gamma}{K_{0}}}
be the twisted level structure induced from
[\varepsilon_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{p}}]_{K_{0}}
by Proposition
3.11
. Then the tuple
(\mathcal{G}^{\gamma},\lambda^{\gamma}_{S},[\varepsilon^{\gamma}_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{p}}]_{{}^{\gamma}{K_{0}}})
is the desired
twisted degenerating family
.

3.2.5.

Let us recall the construction of Hecke twists on
\mathcal{S}_{K_{0}}
and
\mathcal{S}_{K_{0}}^{\Sigma_{0}}
; see
[
44
, 6.4.3]
.
Choose any
g\in G_{0}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
such that
K_{0}^{p,g}:={(K_{0}^{p})}^{g}
stabilizes some
\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{p}
-lattice
V^{\prime}_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{p}}\subset V_{\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p}}
. Recall that
\nu(g)
can be uniquely decomposed as
\nu(g)=r(g)h(g)
for some
r(g)\in\mathbb{Z}_{(p),>0}^{\times}
and
h(g)\in\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{p,\times}
.

Consider the sequence

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{p}}^{\prime}\subset V_{\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V_{\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V^{p}\mathcal{A}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g\cdot}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varepsilon_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{p}}\otimes 1}

Then the image
(\varepsilon_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{p}}\otimes 1)\circ g(V_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{p}}^{\prime})
of
V_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{p}}
determines a
\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}^{\times}
-isogeny
f:(\mathcal{A},\lambda)\longrightarrow(\mathcal{A}^{g},\lambda^{\prime\prime})
.
Since the map

g:(V_{\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p}},\psi_{\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p}})\longrightarrow(V_{\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p}},\nu(g)^{-1}\psi_{\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p}})

sends
\psi_{\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p}}
to
\nu(g)^{-1}\psi_{\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p}}
, we adjust
\lambda^{\prime\prime}
to
\lambda^{g}:=r(g)^{-1}\lambda^{\prime\prime}
, then
f\circ(\varepsilon_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{p}}\otimes 1)\circ(g\cdot)
sends
\psi_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{p}}
to a
\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{p,\times}
-multiple of
\mathrm{e}^{\lambda^{g}}
.
By Lemma
3.15
and
[
21
, I. Prop. 2.7]
and as the proof of Proposition
3.14
,
(\mathcal{A}^{g},\lambda^{g})
extends uniquely to a pair
(\mathcal{G}^{g},\lambda^{g}_{S})
of a semi-abelian scheme
\mathcal{G}^{g}
and an isogeny
\lambda^{g}_{S}
over
S
. The tuple
(\mathcal{G}^{g},\lambda^{g}_{S},[\varepsilon^{g}_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{p}}]_{K^{p,g}_{0}})
is the desired Hecke twist of the degenerating family
(\mathcal{G},\lambda_{S},[\varepsilon_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{p}}]_{K^{p}_{0}})
.

Let
g\in G_{0}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
and let
\gamma\in G_{0}^{\mathrm{ad}}({\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}})_{1}
be chosen as above.
Following the sign convention in
[
36
, 3.4.4]
and
[
33
, 4.5.6]
, we convert the left action of
G_{0}^{\mathrm{ad}}({\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}})_{1}
to a right action: the right action of
(g,\gamma^{-1})\in G_{0}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})\times G_{0}^{\mathrm{ad}}({\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}})_{1}
is given by

(\mathcal{G},\lambda_{S},[\varepsilon_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{p}}]_{K^{p}_{0}})\mapsto((\mathcal{G}^{g})^{\gamma},(\lambda^{g})^{\gamma},[(\varepsilon^{g}_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{p}})^{\gamma}]_{{}^{\gamma}{(K^{p,g}_{0})}}).

3.3.
Twisting
1
-motives

In this subsection, we describe the twisting action on
1
-motives with additional structures.
Note that this construction is similar to, but is not a direct consequence of, the last subsection.

3.3.1.

Let us first record Lemma
3.16
, which serves as the counterpart to
[
36
, Lem. 3.2.2]
and
[
33
, Lem. 4.5.2]
for
1
-motives.
Let
Z_{0,{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}
and
G_{0,{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}
be the groups defined in §
3.2.1
. Recall that
Z_{0}(\mathbb{C})\hookrightarrow G_{0}(\mathbb{C})\hookrightarrow\mathrm{GL}(V_{\mathbb{C}})
induces an action of
Z_{0}(\mathbb{C})
on
V_{\mathbb{C}}
. Fixing an
x\in D_{\Phi_{0}}\subset D_{\Phi^{\ddagger}}
, the integral mixed Hodge structure
\mathbb{M}_{\mathrm{MH}}(x)
corresponds to a
1
-motive
\mathcal{Q}_{x}
. The action of
Z_{0}(\mathbb{Q})
on
\mathbf{M}_{\mathrm{MH}}(x)
induces an embedding

Z_{0}(\mathbb{Q})\hookrightarrow\underline{\mathrm{Aut}}_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathbf{M}_{\mathrm{MH}}(x)\xrightarrow[Thm.\ref{thm-deligne-iii}]{\sim}\underline{\mathrm{Aut}}_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathcal{Q}_{x}.

The action of
Z_{0}(\mathbb{C})
on
\mathbf{M}_{\mathrm{MH}}(x)\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{C}
induces an embedding
Z_{0}(\mathbb{C})\hookrightarrow\underline{\mathrm{Aut}}_{\mathbb{C}}\mathcal{Q}_{x}
.

Let us fix a
\wp=[(x,\mathbf{p})]
, where
\mathbf{p}\in P_{\Phi_{0}}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
. Since there is a
\mathbb{Q}^{\times}
-isogeny
f:\mathcal{Q}_{x}\to\mathcal{Q}_{\wp}
,
f
induces an isomorphism
\mathbf{M}_{\mathrm{MH}}(x)\cong T_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathcal{Q}_{x}\otimes\mathbb{Q}\cong T_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathcal{Q}_{\wp}\otimes\mathbb{Q}
. So there is an embedding
Z_{0}(\mathbb{Q})\hookrightarrow\underline{\mathrm{Aut}}_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathbf{M}_{\mathrm{MH}}(x)\cong\underline{\mathrm{Aut}}_{\mathbb{Q}}T_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathcal{Q}_{\wp}\cong\underline{\mathrm{Aut}}_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathcal{Q}_{\wp}
; similarly, there is an embedding for
\mathbb{C}
-points,
Z_{0}(\mathbb{C})\hookrightarrow\underline{\mathrm{Aut}}_{\mathbb{C}}\mathcal{Q}_{\wp}
. Those embeddings do not depend on the choice of liftings
(x,\mathbf{p})
of
\wp
. Moreover, we have

Lemma 3.16
.

Let
\Phi_{0}
and
\Phi^{\ddagger}
be the cusp label representatives defined as in §
2.4
.
Let
\mathcal{Q}
be the pullback of the tautological
1
-motive
\mathcal{Q}^{\ddagger}
from
\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi^{\ddagger}}}
to
\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{0}}}
. Then there is an embedding
\mathbf{z}_{\Phi_{0}}:Z_{0,{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}\hookrightarrow\underline{\mathrm{Aut}}_{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}\mathcal{Q}
, such that the pullback to
\wp
of the
\mathbb{C}
-points of
\mathbf{z}_{\Phi_{0}}
is the embedding above.

Proof.

The arguments are similar to those in
[
36
, Lem. 3.2.2]
and
[
33
, Lem. 4.5.2]
.
For any
1
-motive
\mathcal{Q}
over
\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{0}}}
, denote by
\mathcal{Q}_{\eta}
the generic fiber of
\mathcal{Q}
over
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi_{0}}}
.

First, let us show that there is an embedding
Z_{0}\hookrightarrow\underline{\mathrm{Aut}}_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathcal{Q}_{\eta}
. The idea is completely the same as
[
36
, Lem. 3.2.2]
.
Let
\wp=[(x,\mathbf{p})]\in\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi_{0}}}(\mathbb{C})
. For any
(x,\mathbf{p})
lifting
\wp
,
x
defines an embedding
e_{x}:G_{0}(\mathbb{C})\hookrightarrow\underline{\mathrm{Aut}}_{\mathbb{C}}\mathcal{Q}_{x}\cong\underline{\mathrm{Aut}}_{\mathbb{C}}\mathbf{M}_{\mathrm{MH}}(x)
. This embedding depends only on
x
. For any other
x^{\prime}\in D_{\Phi_{0}}
,
e_{x}
and
e_{x^{\prime}}
differ by a conjugation of an element in
P_{\Phi_{0}}(\mathbb{R})U_{\Phi_{0}}(\mathbb{C})
. Hence, the embedding of
Z_{0}(\mathbb{C})\hookrightarrow G_{0}(\mathbb{C})
composed with
e_{x}
does not depend on the choice of
x\in D_{\Phi_{0}}
. Thus, there is a well-defined embedding
Z(\mathbb{C})\hookrightarrow\underline{\mathrm{Aut}}_{\mathbb{C}}\mathcal{Q}_{\eta}(\mathbb{C})
, which induces an embedding
Z_{0}\hookrightarrow\underline{\mathrm{Aut}}_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathcal{Q}_{\eta}(\mathbb{C})
.

Let
E_{0}:=E(G_{0},X_{0})=E(P_{\Phi_{0}},D_{\Phi_{0}})
(see
[
64
, Prop. 12.1]
).
Since
\mathcal{Q}_{\eta}[n](\mathbb{C})
and
\underline{\mathrm{Aut}}\mathcal{Q}_{\eta}[n](\mathbb{C})
are defined over
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi_{0}},\overline{E}_{0}}
, so are
T_{p}\mathcal{Q}_{\eta}(\mathbb{C})
and
\underline{\mathrm{Aut}}T_{p}\mathcal{Q}_{\eta}(\mathbb{C})
. So the action of
\mathrm{Aut}(\mathbb{C}/E_{0})
on
T_{p}\mathcal{Q}_{\eta}(\mathbb{C})
and
\underline{\mathrm{Aut}}T_{p}\mathcal{Q}_{\eta}(\mathbb{C})
factors through
\mathrm{Aut}(\overline{E}_{0}/E_{0})
. Also, since
\underline{\mathrm{Aut}}(\mathcal{Q}_{\wp}(\mathbb{C}))\hookrightarrow\underline{\mathrm{Aut}}(T_{p}\mathcal{Q}_{\wp}(\mathbb{C}))
at any
\wp
(see Lemma
2.15
),
\underline{\mathrm{Aut}}\mathcal{Q}_{\eta}(\mathbb{C})\cong\underline{\mathrm{Aut}}\mathcal{Q}_{\eta}(\overline{E}_{0})
.

Let
(T,X_{T})
be a Shimura datum of a
\mathbb{Q}
-torus
T
with an embedding
\mathbf{sp}_{T}:(T,X_{T})\to(P_{\Phi_{0}},D_{\Phi_{0}})
. Denote by
E_{T}
the reflex field of
(T,X_{T})
. The projection of
\mathbf{sp}_{T}
under
P_{\Phi_{0}}\to P_{\Phi_{0},h}
defines a special point
\mathbf{sp}_{T,h}:(T,X_{T})\to(P_{\Phi_{0},h},D_{\Phi_{0},h})
, and we can assume
T
is maximal without loss of generality. Denote by
\mathcal{Q}_{X_{T}}
the
1
-motive determined by
X_{T}\hookrightarrow D_{\Phi_{0}}
. For any
\tau\in\mathrm{Gal}(\overline{E}_{T}/E_{T})
, by the description in
[
64
, 11.4, p.188]
,
Z_{0}(R)
commutes with
\tau
for any finite
\mathbb{Q}
-algebra
R
when we view them as elements in
\underline{\mathrm{Aut}}_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathcal{Q}_{X_{T},\overline{E}_{T}}
.
Consider the orbit of conjugation of
\mathbf{sp}_{T}
by
P_{\Phi_{0}}(\mathbb{Q})
in the orbit of conjugation of it by
P_{\Phi_{0}}(\mathbb{R})U_{\Phi_{0}}(\mathbb{C})
. As
W_{\Phi_{0}}(\mathbb{Q})
is dense in
W_{\Phi_{0}}(\mathbb{R})U_{\Phi_{0}}(\mathbb{C})
under the topology induced by Zariski topology on
W_{\Phi_{0}}
, as
P_{\Phi,h}(\mathbb{Q})
is dense (and in particular, Zariski dense) in
P_{\Phi_{0},h}(\mathbb{R})
, and as
P_{\Phi_{0}}(\mathbb{R})U_{\Phi_{0}}(\mathbb{C})^{+}
acts transitively on
D_{\Phi_{0}}^{+}
,
\mathrm{Gal}(\overline{E}_{T}/E_{T})
commutes with
Z_{0}(R)
on the connected component of
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi_{0}},\overline{E}_{0}}
containing
\mathbf{sp}_{T}
; since the action of
\mathbf{p}\in P_{\Phi_{0}}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
is transitive on connected components of
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi_{0}},\overline{E}_{0}}
and is defined over
E_{0}
, this is true for all connected components.
By
[
17
, 5.1]
and
[
64
, Lem. 11.6]
(by varying special points), the action of
\mathrm{Gal}(\overline{E}_{0}/E_{0})
commutes with that of
Z_{0}(R)
in
\underline{\mathrm{Aut}}_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathcal{Q}_{\eta,\overline{E}_{0}}
.

Finally, let us show the statement over the integral model
\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{0}}}
.
By Lemma
3.18
below, any endomorphism of
\mathcal{Q}_{\eta}
extends uniquely to an endomorphism of
\mathcal{Q}
. Then there is an embedding
Z_{0}\hookrightarrow\underline{\mathrm{Aut}}_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathcal{Q}
. For any point
\wp=[(x,\mathbf{p})]\in\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi_{0}}}(\mathbb{C})
as above, there is a map
Z_{0,{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}\to G_{0,{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}\to\underline{\mathrm{Aut}}_{{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}\mathcal{Q}_{x}\cong\underline{\mathrm{Aut}}_{{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}\mathbb{M}_{\mathrm{MH}}(x)\to\mathrm{GL}(V_{{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}})
; since the composition is injective, the map
Z_{0,{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}\hookrightarrow\underline{\mathrm{Aut}}_{{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}\mathcal{Q}_{x}
is injective. Then there is an embedding
Z_{0,{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}\hookrightarrow\underline{\mathrm{Aut}}_{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}\mathcal{Q}
.
∎

Remark 3.17
.

Since any homomorphism between
1
-motives respects weight filtrations and graded pieces,
\mathbf{z}_{\Phi_{0}}
induces actions
\mathbf{z}_{\Phi_{0},-i}:Z_{0,{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}\to\underline{\mathrm{Aut}}_{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}W_{-i}\mathcal{Q}
and
\gr_{-i}\mathbf{z}_{\Phi_{0}}:Z_{0,{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}\to\underline{\mathrm{Aut}}_{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}\gr^{W}_{-i}\mathcal{Q}
.

Lemma 3.18

(See also
[
21
, I. Prop. 2.7]
)
.

Let
\mathcal{Q}_{1}
and
\mathcal{Q}_{2}
be two
1
-motives over
S
, where
S
is a locally Noetherian and normal scheme. Let
U
be an open dense subscheme of
S
. Suppose that
f_{U}:\mathcal{Q}_{1,U}\to\mathcal{Q}_{2,U}
is a homomorphism between
1
-motives over
U
. Then
f_{U}
uniquely extends to a homomorphism

f:\mathcal{Q}_{1}\longrightarrow\mathcal{Q}_{2}.

Proof.

f_{U}^{et}:\underline{Y}_{\mathcal{Q}_{1},U}\to\underline{Y}_{\mathcal{Q}_{2},U}
extends uniquely to a homomorphism
f^{et}
over
S
. By
[
21
, Prop. 2.7]
,
f^{sab}_{U}:\mathcal{G}^{\natural}_{\mathcal{Q}_{1},U}\to\mathcal{G}^{\natural}_{\mathcal{Q}_{2},U}
extends uniquely to
f^{sab}:\mathcal{G}^{\natural}_{\mathcal{Q}_{1}}\to\mathcal{G}^{\natural}_{\mathcal{Q}_{2}}
. Since
\mathcal{G}^{\natural}_{\mathcal{Q}_{2}}
is separated and
\underline{Y}_{1}
is reduced,
f^{sab}\circ\iota_{\mathcal{Q}_{1}}=\iota_{\mathcal{Q}_{2}}\circ f^{et}
. So
f=(f^{et},f^{sab})
is a homomorphism.
∎

3.3.2.

Let
G_{0,{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}^{\mathrm{ad}}
,
\gamma\in G_{0,{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}^{\mathrm{ad}}({\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}})_{1}
, the
Z_{0,{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}
-torsor
\mathcal{P}
at
\gamma
, the finite Galois extension
F
over
\mathbb{Q}
, the lifting
\tilde{\gamma}\in\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{O}_{F,(p)})
, the
1
-cocycle
\{z^{\tilde{\gamma}}_{\sigma}\}_{\sigma}
, and the
\mathbb{Z}
-module
\mathbf{V}_{\mathbb{Z}}
be defined as in §
3.2.2
and §
3.1.1
.

Denote by
\mathcal{Q}^{\mathcal{O}_{F}}:=\mathrm{Hom}(\mathcal{O}_{F}^{\vee},\mathcal{Q})=(\underline{Y}^{\mathcal{O}_{F}},\mathcal{G}^{\natural,{\mathcal{O}_{F}}},T^{\mathcal{O}_{F}},A^{\mathcal{O}_{F}},\iota^{\mathcal{O}_{F}},c^{\vee,{\mathcal{O}_{F}}})
the
Serre tensor construction
for
1
-motives. As before, let
\mathcal{Q}
be the pullback to
\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{0}}}
of the tautological family
\mathcal{Q}^{\ddagger}
on
\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi^{\ddagger}}}
. It makes sense to write
\mathcal{Q}^{\mathcal{O}_{F}}
as
\mathcal{Q}\otimes{\mathcal{O}_{F}}
viewing them as complexes in
D^{b}_{fppf}(\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{0}}})
.

As in §
3.1.1
, there is an action
\iota_{\Phi,\mathrm{Gal}}:\mathrm{Gal}(F/\mathbb{Q})\to\underline{\mathrm{Aut}}_{{\mathcal{O}_{F,(p)}}}(\mathcal{Q})\cong\underline{\mathrm{Aut}}_{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}(\mathcal{Q}\otimes{\mathcal{O}_{F}})\cong\underline{\mathrm{Aut}}_{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}(\mathcal{Q}^{\mathcal{O}_{F}})
induced by
\mathbf{z}_{\Phi_{0}}
and
\tilde{\gamma}
, sending
\sigma\in\mathrm{Gal}(F/\mathbb{Q})
to
\mathbf{z}_{\Phi_{0}}(z_{\sigma}^{\tilde{\gamma}})
; as before, we will omit
\mathbf{z}_{\Phi_{0}}
if it is clear in the context.

Construction 3.19
.

Fix an
N>0
such that
(N,p)=1
and
Nz^{\tilde{\gamma}}_{\sigma}\in\underline{\mathrm{Aut}}\mathcal{Q}^{\mathcal{O}_{F}}
. We consider a similar homomorphism

\mathbf{f}^{\tilde{\gamma}}_{N}:=\bigoplus\limits_{\sigma\in\mathrm{Gal}(F/\mathbb{Q})\text{ and }\sigma\neq\mathrm{id}}((Nz_{\sigma}^{\tilde{\gamma}})\sigma-N\cdot\mathrm{id}),

which is a homomorphism from
\mathcal{Q}^{{\mathcal{O}_{F}}}
to
(\mathcal{Q}^{{\mathcal{O}_{F}}})^{|\mathrm{Gal}(F/\mathbb{Q})|-1}
.

By Proposition
3.3
,
(\ker\mathbf{f}^{\tilde{\gamma},ab}_{N})^{\circ}=:A^{\tilde{\gamma}}
is an abelian scheme, and
(\ker\mathbf{f}^{\tilde{\gamma},tr}_{N})^{\circ}:=T^{\tilde{\gamma}}
is a torus. Since the kernel of any homomorphism between locally constant groups is locally constant,
\ker\mathbf{f}^{\tilde{\gamma},et}_{N}
is a locally constant abelian group of finite rank. We see that
\mathcal{G}^{\natural,\tilde{\gamma}}:=(\ker\mathbf{f}^{\tilde{\gamma},sab}_{N})^{\circ}
is a semi-abelian scheme, which is an extension of
A^{\tilde{\gamma}}
by
T^{\tilde{\gamma}}
by the proof of Lemma
3.13
. Let
\underline{Y}^{\tilde{\gamma}}:=\iota^{{\mathcal{O}_{F}},-1}\mathcal{G}^{\natural,\tilde{\gamma}}\cap\ker\mathbf{f}^{\tilde{\gamma},et}_{N}
.

Then the homomorphism
\iota^{{\mathcal{O}_{F}}}
induces a homomorphism
\iota^{\tilde{\gamma}}:\underline{Y}^{\tilde{\gamma}}\to\mathcal{G}^{\natural,\tilde{\gamma}}
. This defines a
1
-motive
\mathcal{Q}^{\tilde{\gamma}}:=(\underline{Y}^{\tilde{\gamma}},\mathcal{G}^{\natural,\tilde{\gamma}},T^{\tilde{\gamma}},A^{\tilde{\gamma}},\iota^{\tilde{\gamma}})
, which does not depend on the choice of
N
satisfying the requirements above: To see this, let
M
be another positive integer, such that
N|M
and
(M,p)=1
. Then
\mathbf{f}^{\tilde{\gamma}}_{M}=\frac{M}{N}\mathbf{f}^{\tilde{\gamma}}_{N}
. We have that
\mathcal{Q}^{\tilde{\gamma}}/\mathcal{Q}^{\tilde{\gamma}}[\frac{M}{N}]\cong\mathcal{Q}^{\tilde{\gamma}}.
See Lemma
2.14
.

Lemma 3.20
.

The kernel of
\mathbf{f}^{\tilde{\gamma}}_{N}
induces an isomorphism
\mathcal{Q}^{\tilde{\gamma}}\otimes{\mathcal{O}_{F,(p)}}\cong\mathcal{Q}^{{\mathcal{O}_{F}}}\otimes{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}
of complexes in
D^{b}_{fppf}(\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{0}}})
. Moreover,

\bm{\iota}^{\tilde{\gamma}}_{N}:\mathcal{Q}^{\tilde{\gamma},{\mathcal{O}_{F}}}\longrightarrow\mathcal{Q}^{{\mathcal{O}_{F}}}\otimes{{\mathcal{O}_{F}}}\longrightarrow\mathcal{Q}^{{\mathcal{O}_{F}}}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{F}}\mathcal{O}_{F}\cong\mathcal{Q}^{\mathcal{O}_{F}}

is a prime-to-
p
isogeny.

Proof.

By (
3.1
) and Corollary
3.5
, the quotient
(\ker{\mathbf{f}}_{N}^{\tilde{\gamma},sab})/\mathcal{G}^{\natural,\tilde{\gamma}}
is trivial after tensoring
{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}
, so it is prime-to-
p
. Then the index of
\underline{Y}^{\tilde{\gamma}}
in
\ker{\mathbf{f}}_{N}^{\tilde{\gamma},et}
is prime-to-
p
.
Thus, by decomposing
\mathbf{f}^{\tilde{\gamma}}_{N}
to
\mathbf{f}^{\tilde{\gamma},ab}_{N}
,
\mathbf{f}^{\tilde{\gamma},tr}_{N}
and
\mathbf{f}^{\tilde{\gamma},et}_{N}
, and by Corollary
3.5
(resp. Proposition
3.3
), we have the first (resp. second) statement.
∎

The following statement is an immediate corollary of (the proof of) Lemma
3.16
; we have re-interpreted a similar statement in Lemma
3.7
.

Corollary 3.21
.

Let
(\mathcal{Q},\bm{\lambda},[u]_{(K^{p}_{\Phi_{0}})^{g^{\ddagger}}})
be the pullback of the tautological family on
\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi^{\ddagger}}}
to
\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{0}}}
. Let
R
be a
{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}
-algebra contained in
\mathbb{C}
.

On the one hand, the embedding
Z_{0,{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}\hookrightarrow G_{0,{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{GL}(V_{{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}})
induces an action of
Z_{0,{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}(R)
on
V_{\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p}}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}R
; on the other hand, the embedding
