\mathbf{z}_{\Phi_{0}}:Z_{0,{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}\hookrightarrow\underline{\mathrm{Aut}}_{{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}\mathcal{Q}
induces an action of
Z_{0,{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}(R)
on
V^{p}\mathcal{Q}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}R
.

Then
u\otimes 1:V_{\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}R\xrightarrow{\ \sim\ }V^{p}\mathcal{Q}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}R
is
Z_{0,{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}(R)
-equivariant.

Proof.

We can assume that
R=\mathbb{C}
and can just consider the generic fiber
\mathcal{Q}_{\eta}
over
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi_{0}}}
without loss of generality.

In the notation of Construction
2.45
, for any lifting
(x,\mathbf{p})
of
\wp=[(x,\mathbf{p})]\in\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi_{0}}}(\mathbb{C})
, the map
u_{\wp}
fits into a diagram

(3.8)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V_{\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p}}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}\mathbb{C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V^{p}\mathcal{Q}_{\wp}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}\mathbb{C}\cong H_{\mathrm{dR}}\mathcal{Q}_{x}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V_{\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}\mathbb{C}\cong V_{\mathbb{C}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{\wp}\otimes 1}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p^{{\ddagger},p}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha_{x}\otimes 1}

Since
p^{{\ddagger},p}
commutes with
Z(\mathbb{C})
, it suffices to show that
\alpha_{x}\otimes 1
is
Z(\mathbb{C})
-equivariant. By Lemma
3.16
and its proof, the
\mathbb{C}
-value of
\mathbf{z}_{\Phi_{0}}
is given by the variation of mixed Hodge structure on
V_{\mathbb{C}}
.
Fix any point
x_{0}\in D_{\Phi_{0}}
. We can choose
\alpha_{x_{0}}
to be the identity. Then
\alpha_{x}
is induced by multiplying some element in
P_{\Phi_{0}}(\mathbb{C})
, which commutes with
Z(\mathbb{C})
.
∎

Now, we can construct the twisting of
(\mathcal{Q},\bm{\lambda},[u]_{(K_{\Phi_{0}})^{g^{\ddagger}}})
.

Construction 3.22
.

Consider

(3.9)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbf{V}_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{p}}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{F}\subset\mathbf{V}_{\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p}}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}\mathcal{O}_{F,(p)}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{V_{\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p}}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}\mathcal{O}_{F,(p)}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V^{p}\mathcal{Q}^{{\mathcal{O}_{F}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V^{p}\mathcal{Q}^{\prime},}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces({\tilde{\gamma}}^{-1}\cdot)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u\otimes 1}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V^{p}f}

where
f:\mathcal{Q}^{{\mathcal{O}_{F}}}\to\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}
is a
\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}^{\times}
-isogeny determined by Lemma
2.44
.

(1)

We can claim that
(T^{p}\mathcal{Q}^{\prime})^{\mathrm{Gal}(F/\mathbb{Q})}
comes from the Tate module of a
1
-motive
\mathcal{Q}^{\tilde{\gamma},\prime}
, such that
\mathcal{Q}^{\tilde{\gamma},\prime}
is
\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}^{\times}
-isogenous to
\mathcal{Q}^{\tilde{\gamma}}
. In fact, by Corollary
3.21
, we take the
\mathrm{Gal}(F/\mathbb{Q})
-invariant part of both
\mathbf{V}_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{p}}\otimes{\mathcal{O}_{F}}
and
T^{p}\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}
, and we have that

\mathbf{V}_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{p}}
is an open compact subgroup of
V^{p}\mathcal{Q}^{\tilde{\gamma}}
under
(u\otimes 1)\circ(\tilde{\gamma}^{-1})
, such that
(u\otimes 1)\circ(\tilde{\gamma}^{-1})(\mathbf{V}_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{p}})_{\overline{s}}
is
\pi_{1}(S,\overline{s})
-invariant. Hence, there is a
\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}^{\times}
-isogeny

f^{\tilde{\gamma}}:\mathcal{Q}^{\tilde{\gamma}}\longrightarrow\mathcal{Q}^{\tilde{\gamma},\prime}

by Lemma
2.44
.

This construction only depends on
\gamma
, but does not depend on the lifting
\tilde{\gamma}
. This is by the same argument as Lemma
3.9
. In fact , if
\tilde{\gamma}^{\prime}=z\tilde{\gamma}
, there is a commutative diagram

(3.10)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{V}_{\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p}}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}\mathcal{O}_{F,(p)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V_{\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p}}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}\mathcal{O}_{F,(p)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V^{p}\mathcal{Q}^{\mathcal{O}_{F}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V_{\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p}}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}\mathcal{O}_{F,(p)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V^{p}\mathcal{Q}^{\mathcal{O}_{F}}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces({\tilde{\gamma}}^{-1}\cdot)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces({\tilde{\gamma}}^{\prime,-1}\cdot)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces z^{-1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u\otimes 1}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces z^{-1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u\otimes 1}

The images of
\mathbf{V}_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{p}}
in
V^{p}\mathcal{Q}^{{\mathcal{O}_{F}}}
under
(u\otimes 1)\circ(\tilde{\gamma}^{-1}\cdot)
and
(u\otimes 1)\circ(\tilde{\gamma}^{\prime,-1}\cdot)
differ by
z^{-1}
. Thus, they determine the same
\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}
and
\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}^{\times}
-isogenies
f_{1}:\mathcal{Q}^{{\mathcal{O}_{F}}}\to\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}
and
f_{2}:\mathcal{Q}^{{\mathcal{O}_{F}}}\to\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}
such that
f_{1}=f_{2}\circ z^{-1}
.

Then we can define the twisted level structure as
[u^{\gamma}]_{(K_{\Phi_{0}})^{g^{{\ddagger}}\gamma^{-1}}}
, where
u^{\gamma}:=(V^{p}f\circ(u\otimes 1)\circ(\tilde{\gamma}^{-1}\cdot))^{\mathrm{Gal}(F/\mathbb{Q})}|_{\mathbf{V}_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{p}}}
. This is indeed independent of choice of
\tilde{\gamma}
lifting
\gamma
by the paragraph above. Let
\mathcal{Q}^{\gamma}:=\mathcal{Q}^{\tilde{\gamma},\prime}
. We will see later that this is indeed a level structure as Definition
2.38
.

(2)

We now define the twisted polarization. The treatment is similar to that in §
3.2.3
, so we will use the notation there.
More precisely, consider the diagram

(3.11)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbf{V}_{\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p}}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}\mathcal{O}_{F,(p)}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V_{\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p}}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}\mathcal{O}_{F,(p)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V^{p}\mathcal{Q}^{{\mathcal{O}_{F}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V^{p}\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbf{V}_{\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p}}^{\vee}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}\mathcal{O}_{F,(p)}(\nu)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{V_{\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p}}^{\vee}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}\mathcal{O}_{F,(p)}(\nu)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V^{p}\mathcal{Q}^{\vee}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}\mathcal{O}_{F,(p)}(\nu)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V^{p}\mathcal{Q}^{*}(\nu)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\tilde{\gamma}^{-1}\cdot)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\psi^{\mathbf{V}}\otimes 1}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u\otimes 1}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\psi\otimes 1}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V^{p}f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V^{p}{\bm{\lambda}}\otimes 1}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V^{p}{\bm{\lambda}}^{\mathbf{V},{\mathcal{O}_{F}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V^{p}f^{\prime}}

The image of
\mathbf{V}_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{p}}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{F}
(resp.
\mathbf{V}_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{p}}^{\vee}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{F}
) in
V^{p}\mathcal{Q}^{{\mathcal{O}_{F}}}
(resp.
V^{p}\mathcal{Q}^{\vee}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}\mathcal{O}_{F,(p)}
) via
(u\otimes 1)\circ(\tilde{\gamma}^{-1}\cdot)
(resp.
(V^{p}\bm{\lambda}\otimes 1)\circ(u\otimes 1)\circ(\tilde{\gamma}^{-1}\cdot)\circ(\psi^{\mathbf{V}}\otimes 1)
) determines a

\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}^{\times}
-isogeny
f
(resp.
f^{\prime}
) together with a prime-to-
p
isogeny
\bm{\lambda}^{\mathbf{V},{\mathcal{O}_{F}}}:\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}\to\mathcal{Q}^{*}
by Lemma
2.44
such that the right square commutes.

By Corollary
3.21
,
u\otimes 1
is
Z_{0,{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}(\mathcal{O}_{F,(p)})
-equivariant. Since
u
sends
\psi_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{p}}
to a
\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{p,\times}
-multiple of
\mathrm{e}^{\bm{\lambda}}
,
V^{p}\bm{\lambda}\otimes 1
,
V^{p}f^{\prime}
and
V^{p}\bm{\lambda}^{\mathbf{V},{\mathcal{O}_{F}}}
are
Z_{0,{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}(\mathcal{O}_{F,(p)})
-equivariant and therefore are
\mathrm{Gal}(F/\mathbb{Q})
-equivariant.

Note that, for
\mathcal{Q}^{\vee}
, we can also define a
1
-motive
(\mathcal{Q}^{\vee})^{\tilde{\gamma}}
by the
Z_{0,{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}
-action given by the canonical isomorphism (
B.2
). Since

V^{p}(\mathcal{Q}^{\vee})^{\tilde{\gamma}}=(V^{p}{\bm{\lambda}}^{\mathbf{V},{\mathcal{O}_{F}}}\circ V^{p}f\circ(u\otimes 1)\circ({\tilde{\gamma}}^{-1}\cdot)\circ(\psi^{\mathbf{V}}\otimes 1)^{-1})(\mathbf{V}^{\vee}_{\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p}}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}\mathcal{O}_{F,(p)}(\nu))^{\mathrm{Gal}(F/\mathbb{Q})},

the image of
\mathbf{V}^{\vee}_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{p}}
in
V^{p}(\mathcal{Q}^{\vee})^{\tilde{\gamma}}
induces a
\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}^{\times}
-isogeny
(\mathcal{Q}^{\vee})^{\tilde{\gamma}}\to\mathcal{Q}^{\prime\prime}
such that
T^{p}\mathcal{Q}^{\prime\prime}=(T^{p}\mathcal{Q}^{*})^{\mathrm{Gal}(F/\mathbb{Q})}
. There is also a prime-to-
p
isogeny
\bm{\lambda}^{\tilde{\gamma},\prime}:\mathcal{Q}^{\gamma}\to\mathcal{Q}^{\prime\prime}
which is the
\mathrm{Gal}(F/\mathbb{Q})
-invariant of
\bm{\lambda}^{\mathbf{V},{\mathcal{O}_{F}}}:\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}\to\mathcal{Q}^{*}
.

We now check that
\bm{\lambda}^{\tilde{\gamma},\prime}
is a polarization. Indeed, choose any positive definite perfect pairing
\mathrm{e}_{0}:{\mathcal{O}_{F}}\times{\mathcal{O}_{F}}\to\mathbb{Z}
that canonically induces
i_{F}:\mathcal{O}_{F}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathbb{Z}}({\mathcal{O}_{F}},\mathbb{Z})
. As in Lemma
3.10
,
\bm{\lambda}(i_{F})=i_{F}\circ\bm{\lambda}
is a polarization implies that
\bm{\lambda}^{\mathbf{V},{\mathcal{O}_{F}}}(i_{F}):\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}\to\mathcal{Q}^{*}\to\mathcal{Q}^{*}\otimes_{{\mathcal{O}_{F}},i_{F}}\mathcal{O}_{F}^{\vee}
is a polarization. By construction, this implies that
\mathcal{Q}^{\gamma}\otimes{\mathcal{O}_{F}}\xrightarrow{\bm{\lambda}^{\tilde{\gamma},\prime}\otimes 1}\mathcal{Q}^{\prime\prime}\otimes{\mathcal{O}_{F}}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathcal{Q}^{\prime\prime}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{F}^{\vee}
is a polarization. Taking the
\mathrm{Gal}(F/\mathbb{Q})
-invariant part, we know that
\mathcal{Q}^{\tilde{\gamma},\prime}\otimes\mathbb{Z}\xrightarrow{\bm{\lambda}^{\tilde{\gamma},\prime}\otimes 1}\mathcal{Q}^{\prime\prime}\otimes\mathbb{Z}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathcal{Q}^{\prime\prime}\otimes\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathbb{Z}}(\mathbb{Z},\mathbb{Z})
and
\bm{\lambda}^{\tilde{\gamma},\prime}
are polarizations.

From now on, denote
\bm{\lambda}^{\gamma}:=\bm{\lambda}^{\tilde{\gamma},\prime}
.

(3)

Let us check that
[u^{\gamma}]_{(K_{\Phi_{0}})^{g^{\ddagger}\gamma^{-1}}}
is a level structure defined as in Definition
2.38
.
By the previous part,
u^{\gamma}
sends
\mathbf{V}_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{p}}
to
T^{p}\mathcal{Q}^{\gamma}
, and sends
\psi^{\mathbf{V}}_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{p}}
to a
\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{p,\times}
-multiple of
\mathrm{e}^{\bm{\lambda}^{\gamma}}
.

Note that
f:\mathcal{Q}^{\mathcal{O}_{F}}\to\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}
respects weight filtrations
W^{{\mathcal{O}_{F}}}
and
W^{\prime}
on
\mathcal{Q}^{{\mathcal{O}_{F}}}
and
\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}
, and respects their graded pieces. Then
f
induces
\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}^{\times}
-isogenies
\gr_{0}f:\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}(\underline{X}^{{\mathcal{O}_{F}}},\mathbb{Z}(1))\to\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}(\underline{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{Z}(1))
and
\gr_{-2}f:\underline{Y}^{{\mathcal{O}_{F}}}\to\underline{Y}^{\prime}
.

Now we can construct the data we need:

•

\mathbf{Z}^{(\gamma g^{\ddagger}\gamma^{-1}),\mathcal{O}_{F}}:=\mathbf{V}_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{p}}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{F}\cap(\tilde{\gamma}^{-1})^{*}Z^{(g^{\ddagger})}_{\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p}}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{F}
; we take
\mathrm{Gal}(F/\mathbb{Q})
-invariant part and obtain a filtration
\mathbf{Z}^{(\gamma g^{\ddagger}\gamma^{-1})}
of
\mathbf{V}_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{p}}
. It is a filtration determined by the parabolic group
\gamma g^{{\ddagger},-1}Qg^{{\ddagger}}\gamma^{-1}
, which explains the superscript in our notation. We denote
\gamma g^{\ddagger}\gamma^{-1}
by
g^{\dagger}
for simplicity.

•

Consider
\alpha_{0}^{\tilde{\gamma},\mathcal{O}_{F,(p)}}:\gr_{0}^{F^{(g^{\dagger})}}\mathbf{V}_{\mathbb{Z}}^{(g^{\dagger})}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{F,(p)}\xrightarrow{\gr_{0}\tilde{\gamma}^{-1}}\gr_{0}^{F^{(g^{\ddagger})}}V_{\mathbb{Z}}^{(g^{\ddagger})}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{F,(p)}\xrightarrow{\alpha_{0}^{(g^{\ddagger})}}\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}(\underline{X},\mathbb{Z}(1))\otimes\mathcal{O}_{F,(p)}\xrightarrow{\gr_{0}f}\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}(\underline{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{Z}(1))\otimes{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}
.
The composition
\alpha^{\tilde{\gamma},\mathcal{O}_{F,(p)}}_{0,\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p}}\circ(\gr_{0}g^{\dagger})_{\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p}}
is
\gr_{0}u^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}_{F}}_{\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p}}
, so it sends
\gr_{0}^{\mathbf{Z}^{(g^{\dagger})}}\mathbf{V}_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{p}}\otimes{\mathcal{O}_{F}}
to
\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}(\underline{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{Z}(1))\otimes\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{p}
.
We then see that the restriction of
\alpha_{0}^{\tilde{\gamma},\mathcal{O}_{F,(p)}}
defines a map
\alpha_{0}^{\tilde{\gamma},{\mathcal{O}_{F}}}:\gr_{0}^{F^{(g^{\dagger})}}\otimes{\mathcal{O}_{F}}\to\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}(\underline{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{Z}(1))
. Taking the
\mathrm{Gal}(F/\mathbb{Q})
-invariant part, there is a map over
\mathbb{Z}
,
\alpha_{0}^{\tilde{\gamma}}:\gr_{0}^{F^{(g^{\dagger})}}\to\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}(\underline{X}^{\gamma},\mathbb{Z}(1))
. Denote
\alpha_{0}^{(g^{\dagger})}:=\alpha_{0}^{\tilde{\gamma}}
.

•

Consider
\alpha_{-2}^{\tilde{\gamma},\mathcal{O}_{F,(p)}}:\gr_{-2}^{F^{(g^{\dagger})}}\mathbf{V}_{\mathbb{Z}}^{(g^{\dagger})}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{F,(p)}\xrightarrow{\gr_{-2}\tilde{\gamma}^{-1}}\gr_{-2}^{F^{(g^{\ddagger})}}V_{\mathbb{Z}}^{(g^{\ddagger})}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{F,(p)}\xrightarrow{\alpha_{0}^{(g^{\ddagger})}}\underline{Y}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{F,(p)}\xrightarrow{\gr_{-2}f}\underline{Y}^{\prime}\otimes{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}
.
The composition
\alpha^{\tilde{\gamma},\mathcal{O}_{F,(p)}}_{-2,\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p}}\circ(\gr_{-2}g^{\dagger})_{\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p}}
is a
\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{p,\times}
-multiple of
\gr_{-2}u^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}_{F}}_{\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p}}
, so it sends
\gr_{-2}^{\mathbf{Z}^{(g^{\dagger})}}\mathbf{V}_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{p}}\otimes{\mathcal{O}_{F}}
to
\underline{Y}^{\prime}\otimes\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{p}
.
We then see that the restriction of
\alpha_{-2}^{\tilde{\gamma},\mathcal{O}_{F,(p)}}
defines a map
\alpha_{-2}^{\tilde{\gamma},{\mathcal{O}_{F}}}:\gr_{-2}^{F^{(g^{\dagger})}}\otimes{\mathcal{O}_{F}}\to\underline{Y}^{\prime}
. Taking the
\mathrm{Gal}(F/\mathbb{Q})
-invariant part, there is a map over
\mathbb{Z}
,
\alpha_{-2}^{\tilde{\gamma}}:\gr_{-2}^{F^{(g^{\dagger})}}\to\underline{Y}^{\gamma}
. Denote
\alpha_{-2}^{(g^{\dagger})}:=\alpha_{-2}^{\tilde{\gamma}}
.

Then
[u^{\gamma}]_{(K_{\Phi_{0}})^{g^{\ddagger}\gamma^{-1}}}
satisfies Definition
2.38
. See Proposition
3.23
below.

Let
\Psi_{0}={}^{\gamma}{\Phi_{0}}:=(\gamma Q\gamma^{-1},\gamma(X_{0}^{+}),\gamma g^{\ddagger}\gamma^{-1})
;
\Psi_{0}
maps to a cusp label representative
\Psi^{\ddagger}
of
(G^{\ddagger},X^{\ddagger})
.
Let
K^{\odot,p}:=K^{{\ddagger},p,\prime}
be any neat open compact subgroup of
G^{\ddagger}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
containing
{}^{\gamma}{K^{p}_{0}}
and stabilizing
\mathbf{V}_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{p}}
. Let
K^{\odot}:=K_{p}^{\ddagger}K^{\odot,p}
.
Denote
K_{\Psi^{\ddagger}}^{\odot,p}:=g^{\dagger}K^{\odot,p}g^{\dagger,-1}\cap P_{\Psi^{\ddagger}}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
. Then we see that
K_{\Psi^{\ddagger}}^{\odot,p}
contains
{}^{\gamma}{K_{\Phi_{0}}^{p}}=K_{\Psi_{0}}^{p}
. In fact, we compute as follows:

g^{\dagger}K^{\odot,p}g^{\dagger,-1}\cap P_{\Psi^{\ddagger}}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})\supset\gamma g^{{\ddagger}}K_{0}^{p}g^{{\ddagger},-1}\gamma^{-1}\cap P_{\Psi^{\ddagger}}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})=\gamma(g^{\ddagger}K_{0}^{p}g^{{\ddagger},-1}\cap P_{\Phi^{\ddagger}}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p}))\gamma^{-1}\supset\gamma K_{\Phi_{0}}^{p}\gamma^{-1}.

Similarly,
K_{\Psi^{\ddagger},p}^{\odot}
contains
{}^{\gamma}{K_{\Phi_{0},p}}=K_{\Psi_{0},p}
. We then have
(K_{\Phi_{0}}^{p})^{g^{\ddagger}\gamma^{-1}}\subset(K_{\Psi^{\ddagger}}^{\odot,p})^{g^{\dagger}}
.

Proposition 3.23
.

With the constructions above,
[u^{\gamma}]_{(K^{\odot,p}_{\Psi^{\ddagger}})^{g^{\dagger}}}:=[u^{\gamma}]_{(K_{\Phi_{0}})^{g^{\ddagger}\gamma^{-1}}}/(K^{\odot,p}_{\Psi^{\ddagger}})^{g^{\dagger}}
is a level structure of type
(\mathbf{V}_{\mathbb{Z}},\psi^{\mathbf{V}}_{\mathbb{Z}},\Psi^{\ddagger},K^{\odot,p})
.

Proof.

This is a consequence of the construction above.
∎

This ends the Construction
3.22
.
\square

3.3.3.

Twisting of
1
-motives sends one cusp label representative of
(G_{0},X_{0})
to another one.

Denote by
\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Psi_{0}}^{p}}^{\odot}
the inverse limit
\varprojlim\mathcal{S}_{K^{\odot}_{\Psi^{\ddagger}}}\cong\varprojlim\bm{\xi}_{(\mathbf{V}_{\mathbb{Z}},\psi^{\mathbf{V}}_{\mathbb{Z}}),\Psi^{\ddagger},K^{\odot,p}}
, where the inverse system runs over neat open compact subgroups
K^{\odot,p}_{\Psi^{\ddagger}}
as above containing
K^{p}_{\Psi_{0}}
; similarly, let
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Psi_{0}}^{p}}^{\odot}:=\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Psi_{0}}^{p},\mathbb{Q}}^{\odot}
.

Proposition 3.24
.

With the constructions above,

(1)

The twisting construction

(\mathcal{Q},\bm{\lambda},[u]_{(K_{\Phi_{0}})^{g^{\ddagger}}})\mapsto(\mathcal{Q}^{\gamma},\bm{\lambda}^{\gamma},[u^{\gamma}]_{(K_{\Phi_{0}})^{g^{\ddagger}\gamma^{-1}}})

induces a morphism
\gamma^{-1}:\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{0}}}\to\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Psi}^{p}}^{\odot}
by the universal property.

(2)

Over
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi_{0}}}(\mathbb{C})\cong P_{\Phi_{0}}(\mathbb{Q})\backslash D_{\Phi_{0}}\times P_{\Phi_{0}}(\mathbb{A}_{f})g^{\ddagger}K/K
,
\gamma^{-1}

is identical to a map
\gamma^{-1}:\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi_{0}}}(\mathbb{C})\to\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Psi}^{p}}^{\odot}(\mathbb{C})
, sending
\wp=[(x,\mathbf{p}g^{\ddagger})]
to
\wp^{\gamma}=[(\gamma(x),\gamma\mathbf{p}g^{\ddagger}\gamma^{-1})]
. Hence, it factors as
\gamma^{-1}:\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi_{0}}}(\mathbb{C})\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Psi_{0}}}(\mathbb{C})\xrightarrow{\iota_{\Psi_{0}}}\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Psi_{0}}^{p}}^{\odot}(\mathbb{C})
, where the first map is an isomorphism sending
\wp=[(x,\mathbf{p}g^{\ddagger})]
to
\widetilde{\wp}^{\gamma}=[(\gamma(x),\gamma\mathbf{p}g^{\ddagger}\gamma^{-1})]
.

(3)

The map
\gamma^{-1}:\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{0}}}\to\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Psi_{0}}^{p}}^{\odot}
factors as
\gamma^{-1}:\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{0}}}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Psi_{0}}}\xrightarrow{\iota_{\Psi_{0}}}\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Psi_{0}}^{p}}^{\odot}
.

Proof.

Given the first and the second part, the third part follows from the construction of
\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Psi_{0}}}
as the normalization of
\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Psi^{\ddagger}}^{\odot}}
in
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Psi_{0}}}
, and the fact that this normalization is independent of the choice of different
K_{\Psi^{\ddagger}}^{\odot,p}
as above. The first part follows from Construction
3.22
.

Let us show the second part. This is the same as Kisin’s argument in
[
36
, Lem. 3.2.6]
, and let us explain it now.

Recall that we are working over
\mathbb{C}
. Let
\mathcal{Q}_{\wp}
be the
1
-motive at a complex point
\wp=[(x,\mathbf{p}g^{\ddagger})]\in\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi_{0}}}(\mathbb{C})
, and let
\mathcal{Q}_{\wp^{\gamma}}
be the
1
-motive corresponding to
\wp^{\gamma}=[(x^{\gamma},\gamma\mathbf{p}g^{\ddagger}\gamma^{-1})]
.
First, there is a diagram:

(3.12)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathcal{Q}^{{\mathcal{O}_{F}}}_{\wp}\otimes{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T_{\mathbb{Z}}(\mathcal{Q}^{\gamma,{\mathcal{O}_{F}}}_{\wp})\otimes{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(V_{\mathbb{Z}}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{F,(p)},r(g^{\ddagger})^{-1}\psi\otimes 1,W)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathbf{V}_{\mathbb{Z}}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{F,(p)},r(g^{\dagger})^{-1}\nu(\tilde{\gamma}^{-1})\psi^{\mathbf{V}}\otimes 1,\mathbf{W}),}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha_{x}\otimes 1}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{\gamma}}

where
W
(resp.
\mathbf{W}
) is the filtration determined by
Q^{\ddagger}
(resp.
\gamma Q^{\ddagger}\gamma^{-1}
) and note that
r(g^{\dagger})=r(g^{\ddagger})
.
Denote
\mathbf{T}:=\tilde{\gamma}\circ(\alpha_{x}\otimes 1)\circ f^{-1}
. By Construction
3.22
and Construction
2.45
,
\mathbf{T}
sends that weight filtration of
T_{\mathbb{Z}}(\mathcal{Q}_{\wp}^{\gamma,{\mathcal{O}_{F}}})\otimes\mathbb{Q}
to
\mathbf{W}
.
The mixed Hodge structures on
V\otimes{\mathcal{O}_{F}}\cong\mathbf{V}\otimes{\mathcal{O}_{F}}
determined by
x
and

x^{\gamma}
viewed as elements in
\mathrm{Hom}(\mathbb{S}_{\mathbb{C}},V_{\mathbb{C}}\otimes{\mathcal{O}_{F}})
are conjugated by
\tilde{\gamma}
. Then the bottom row of the diagram above respects mixed Hodge structures. Hence,
\mathbf{T}
sends the mixed Hodge structure on
T_{\mathbb{Z}}(\mathcal{Q}_{\wp}^{\gamma,{\mathcal{O}_{F}}})\otimes\mathbb{Q}
to the mixed Hodge structure on
\mathbf{V}\otimes{\mathcal{O}_{F}}
which corresponds to
x^{\gamma}
.

Then we combine the diagram above with (
3.9
) and (
2.29
) to get the following commutative diagram:

(3.13)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbf{V}_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{p}}\otimes{\mathcal{O}_{F}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V_{\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p}}\otimes{\mathcal{O}_{F}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathcal{Q}^{{\mathcal{O}_{F}}}_{\wp}\otimes\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T_{\mathbb{Z}}(\mathcal{Q}^{\gamma,{\mathcal{O}_{F}}}_{\wp})\otimes\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(V_{\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p}}\otimes{\mathcal{O}_{F}},\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{p,\times}r(g^{\ddagger})^{-1}\nu(\tilde{\gamma})\psi,W)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathbf{V}_{\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p}}\otimes{\mathcal{O}_{F}},\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{p,\times}r(g^{\dagger})^{-1}\psi^{\mathbf{V}},\mathbf{W}).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{\gamma}^{-1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p^{{\ddagger},p}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha_{x}\otimes 1}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{\gamma}}

The first term is equipped with the pairing
\psi^{\mathbf{V}}\otimes 1
and the second one is equipped with the pairing
\nu(\tilde{\gamma})\psi\otimes 1
.
By adjusting
\mathbf{p}
by an element in
P_{\Phi^{\ddagger}}(\mathbb{Q})
,
we see that the lattice
\mathbf{V}_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{p}}\otimes{\mathcal{O}_{F}}
on the top-left is sent to
T^{p}(\mathcal{Q}^{\gamma,{\mathcal{O}_{F}}}_{\wp})
via the series of morphisms on the first row, and is sent to
\mathbf{V}_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{p}}^{(g^{\dagger})}\otimes{\mathcal{O}_{F}}
via
\tilde{\gamma}^{-1}\circ p^{{\ddagger},p}\circ\tilde{\gamma}
.
Then the second part follows from taking the
\mathrm{Gal}(F/\mathbb{Q})
-invariant of the diagram above and (
2.29
).
∎

3.4.
Some actions of the adjoint group on toroidal compactifications

In this subsection, we extend the twisting construction in previous subsections to toroidal compactifications of integral models of Hodge-type Shimura varieties, and relate another “twisting” construction on the toroidal compactifications of Hodge-type Shimura varieties
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{0}}^{\Sigma}
to quasi-isogeny twists in
[
45
]
. The notation and the construction in this subsection will be used in the proof of one of the main technical results §
4.3.2
.

Let
\gamma\in G^{\mathrm{ad}}_{0}(\mathbb{Q})
and
g\in G_{0}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
. Let
\beta:=(g,\gamma^{-1})
and
(K_{0})^{\beta}:={}^{\gamma}{((K_{0})^{g})}
.
Then
\beta
induces a morphism
\beta:\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{0}}(G_{0},X_{0})\to\mathrm{Sh}_{(K_{0})^{\beta}}(G_{0},\gamma\cdot X_{0})
described by

[(x,g_{0})]\mapsto[(\gamma(x),\gamma g_{0}g\gamma^{-1})]

on complex points.

For any admissible cone decomposition
\Sigma_{2}
of
\mathrm{Sh}_{(K_{0})^{\beta}}:=\mathrm{Sh}_{(K_{0})^{\beta}}(G_{0},\gamma\cdot X_{0})
, let
\Sigma_{1}:=\beta^{*}\Sigma_{2}
be the admissible cone decomposition of
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{0}}:=\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{0}}(G_{0},X_{0})
induced by
\beta
.
By
[
64
, Prop. 6.25 and Thm. 12.4]
, there is a morphism from
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{0}}^{\Sigma_{1}}:=\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{0}}^{\Sigma_{1}}(G_{0},X_{0})
to
\mathrm{Sh}_{(K_{0})^{\beta}}^{\Sigma_{2}}:=\mathrm{Sh}_{(K_{0})^{\beta}}^{\Sigma_{2}}(G_{0},\gamma\cdot X_{0})
extending the morphism of interiors
\beta:\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{0}}(G_{0},X_{0})\to\mathrm{Sh}_{(K_{0})^{\beta}}(G_{0},\gamma\cdot X_{0})
. We still denote this extension by
\beta:\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{0}}^{\Sigma_{1}}\to\mathrm{Sh}^{\Sigma_{2}}_{(K_{0})^{\beta}}
.

Fix a cusp label representative
\Phi_{0}
mapping to
\Phi^{\ddagger}
as before. Then
\beta
induces an action
\beta:\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi_{0}}}\xrightarrow{g}\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi^{g}}}\xrightarrow{\gamma^{-1}}\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Psi^{\prime}}}
, corresponding to an action
(Q,X_{0}^{+},g^{\ddagger})\mapsto\Phi^{g}:=(Q,X^{+}_{0},g^{\ddagger}g)\mapsto\Psi^{\prime}:=(\gamma Q\gamma^{-1},\gamma(X_{0}^{+}),\gamma g^{\ddagger}g\gamma^{-1})
on cusp label representatives. Over complex points, the action of
\beta
sends
[(x,\mathbf{p}g^{\ddagger})]
to
[(\gamma(x),\gamma\mathbf{p}g^{\ddagger}g\gamma^{-1})]
.

3.4.1.

With the constructions in previous subsections, the first result we intended to show is now immediate thanks to a lemma of Madapusi
[
50
, Lem. A.3.4]
. Note that we have fixed a Hodge embedding
\iota:(G_{0},X_{0},K_{0})\hookrightarrow(G^{\ddagger},X^{\ddagger},K^{\ddagger})
as §
2.1
.

Lemma 3.25
.

The action
\beta:\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{0}}^{\Sigma_{1}}\to\mathrm{Sh}^{\Sigma_{2}}_{(K_{0})^{\beta}}
extends to an action

\beta:\mathcal{S}_{K_{0}}^{\Sigma_{1}}\longrightarrow\mathcal{S}^{\Sigma_{2}}_{(K_{0})^{\beta}},

which is compatible with the actions
\beta:\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{0}}}\to\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Psi^{\prime}}}
on the boundaries if
\gamma\in G_{0,{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}^{\mathrm{ad}}({\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}})_{1}
.

Proof.

Combine Proposition
3.24
,
[
64
, Prop. 6.25 and Thm. 12.4(b)]
,
[
50
, Lem. A.3.4]
and
[
50
, 4.1.12]
.
∎

3.4.2.

We now
assume
that
\gamma\in G^{\mathrm{ad}}_{0}(\mathbb{Q})_{1}
and that
g\gamma^{-1}
is trivial in
G^{\mathrm{ad}}_{0}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
.
Under this assumption, we have assumed that
(K_{0})^{\beta}=K_{0}
, and therefore both
\Phi_{0}
and
\Psi^{\prime}
are representatives of some cusps in
\mathrm{Cusp}_{K_{0}}(G_{0},X_{0})
.
We can fix an embedding
\iota:(G_{0},X_{0},K_{0})\hookrightarrow(G^{\ddagger},X^{\ddagger},K^{\ddagger})
satisfying
[
50
, Sec. 3.1]
; applying the theory of
[
50
]
, this choice of the embedding
\iota
has
fixed
a construction of integral models
\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{0}}}
for all
\Phi_{0}\in\mathcal{CLR}(G_{0},X_{0})
.

In the following construction, we will mainly work with
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi_{0}}}
’s instead of their integral models.

Construction 3.26
.

Let
F
be a finite Galois extension of
\mathbb{Q}
such that
\gamma
is lifted to
\tilde{\gamma}\in G_{0}(F)
.

We view
V^{F}:=V\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}F
as a
\mathbb{Q}
-vector space by fixing a
\mathbb{Q}
-isomorphism
V^{F}\xrightarrow{\sim}V^{\oplus[F:\mathbb{Q}]}
.
Denote by
\psi^{F}
the symplectic pairing on
V^{\oplus[F:\mathbb{Q}]}
which is
\psi
on each factor, and elements from distinct factors are perpendicular.

Define
G^{F}:=\mathrm{GSp}(V^{F},\psi^{F})
and let
X^{F}
be the union of Siegel upper and lower half spaces defined by
G^{F}
.

Then there is a sequence of embeddings of Shimura data:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota^{+}:(G_{0},X_{0})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(G^{\ddagger},X^{\ddagger})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(G^{F},X^{F}).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta}

The morphism
(G^{\ddagger},X^{\ddagger})\to(G^{F},X^{F})
is defined by the fixed diagonal embedding.

On the other hand, let
\nu:R_{F/\mathbb{Q}}G^{\ddagger}\to R_{F/\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{G}_{m}
be the similitude character. The value of
\nu(\tilde{\gamma})
remains unchanged if we extend
F
. We then choose
F
to be a finite Galois extension such that it contains the square roots of
\nu(\tilde{\gamma})
. We choose a square root
c(\tilde{\gamma})
of
\nu(\tilde{\gamma})
. We view
c(\tilde{\gamma})
as a
\mathbb{Q}
-point of
R_{F/\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{G}_{m}\hookrightarrow R_{F/\mathbb{Q}}G^{\ddagger}
. Denote
\tilde{\gamma}_{1}:=\tilde{\gamma}^{-1}c(\tilde{\gamma})
. We view
\tilde{\gamma}_{1}
as an element in
\mathrm{GL}(V^{F}).

There is a commutative diagram of algebraic groups:

(3.14)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G^{\ddagger}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G^{F}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{int}(\tilde{\gamma}_{1}^{-1})G^{F}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{int}(\tilde{\gamma}_{1}^{-1})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{int}(\tilde{\gamma}_{1}^{-1})\iota^{+}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{int}(\tilde{\gamma}_{1}^{-1})}

The operation
(\tilde{\gamma}_{1}\cdot)\circ(g\cdot)
on
V^{F}_{\mathbb{A}_{f}}
induces a collection of morphisms

\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi^{F}}}\longrightarrow\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{(\Phi^{F})^{\prime}}},

which sends any cusp label representative
\Phi^{F}:=(Q^{F},X^{F,+},g^{F})
of
(G^{F},X^{F})
to

(\Phi^{F})^{\prime}:=(\tilde{\gamma}_{1}^{-1}Q^{F}{\tilde{\gamma}_{1}},\tilde{\gamma}_{1}^{-1}(X^{F,+}),\tilde{\gamma}_{1}^{-1}g^{F}g\tilde{\gamma}_{1})

of
\mathrm{int}(\tilde{\gamma}_{1}^{-1})(G^{F},X^{F})
.

Over complex points, the map is
[(x^{F},g^{F}_{0}g^{F})]\mapsto[(\tilde{\gamma}_{1}^{-1}x^{F},\tilde{\gamma}_{1}^{-1}g^{F}_{0}g^{F}g\tilde{\gamma}_{1})]
. Note that this action remains unchanged if we change
\tilde{\gamma}_{1}
to
\tilde{\gamma}_{1}\cdot c
for any
c\in\mathbb{G}_{m}(\mathbb{Q})\hookrightarrow G^{F}(\mathbb{Q})
in the center of
G^{F}
. In particular, it remains unchanged if we choose another square root of
\nu(\tilde{\gamma})
.

Suppose that
\Phi_{0}\mapsto\Phi^{\ddagger}\mapsto\Phi^{F}
via
\iota^{+}
and
\Psi^{\prime}\mapsto\Psi^{\prime,{\ddagger}}\mapsto(\Phi^{F})^{\prime}
via
\mathrm{int}(\tilde{\gamma}_{1}^{-1})\iota^{+}
, and if we choose a suitable neat open compact subgroup
K^{F}\subset G^{F}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
containing
K^{\ddagger}
, we have a commutative diagram

(3.15)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi_{0}}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Psi^{\prime}}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi^{\ddagger}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi^{F}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{(\Phi^{F})^{\prime}}}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\beta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota_{\Phi}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathrm{int}(\tilde{\gamma}_{1}^{-1})\iota^{+})_{\Psi^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{int}(\tilde{\gamma}_{1}^{-1})\circ g}

We choose a hyperspecial subgroup
K^{F}_{p}
of
G^{F}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
such that
K^{F}_{p}\cap G^{\ddagger}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})=K^{\ddagger}_{p}
and choose a neat open compact subgroup
K^{F,p}\subset G^{F}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
containing
K^{{\ddagger},p}
and stabilizing
V^{F}_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{p}}:=V_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{p}}\otimes{\mathcal{O}_{F}}
.

Since, by construction,
g^{\ddagger}
maps to
g^{F}
and
Q^{F}
is the unique proper admissible
\mathbb{Q}
-parabolic that contains
Q
and
Q^{\ddagger}
, we have
Q\subset Q^{\ddagger}\subset Q^{F}
. Since the conjugation of
\tilde{\gamma}_{1}^{-1}
on
G_{0}
, viewed as a subgroup of
G^{F}
, is the conjugation of
\tilde{\gamma}
, we have that
\tilde{\gamma}_{1}^{-1}Q^{F}\tilde{\gamma}_{1}
contains
\tilde{\gamma}Q\tilde{\gamma}^{-1}
. Hence,
\tilde{\gamma}_{1}^{-1}Q^{F}\tilde{\gamma}_{1}
is the unique proper admissible
\mathbb{Q}
-parabolic that contains
\tilde{\gamma}Q\tilde{\gamma}^{-1}
since
\tilde{\gamma}_{1}^{-1}Q^{F}\tilde{\gamma}_{1}
depends only on the cocharacter that defines
\tilde{\gamma}Q\tilde{\gamma}^{-1}
(see
[
64
, 4.16]
).

We have the following commutative diagram induced by (
3.15
):

(3.16)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{P_{\Phi_{0}}(\mathbb{A}_{f})\cap g^{\ddagger}K_{0}g^{{\ddagger},-1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\gamma P_{\Phi_{0}}\gamma^{-1}(\mathbb{A}_{f})\cap{}^{\gamma g^{\ddagger}g\gamma^{-1}}{K_{0}}=\gamma P_{\Phi_{0}}\gamma^{-1}(\mathbb{A}_{f})\cap{}^{\gamma g^{{\ddagger}}}{K_{0}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{P_{\Phi^{\ddagger}}(\mathbb{A}_{f})\cap g^{{\ddagger}}K^{\ddagger}g^{{\ddagger},-1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{P_{\Phi^{F}}(\mathbb{A}_{f})\cap g^{\ddagger}K^{F}g^{{\ddagger},-1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P_{(\Phi^{F})^{\prime}}(\mathbb{A}_{f})\cap{}^{\tilde{\gamma}_{1}^{-1}g^{\ddagger}}{K^{F}}.}

It follows from the assumption that
\Psi^{\prime}=(\gamma Q\gamma,\gamma(X^{+}_{0}),\gamma g^{\ddagger}g\gamma^{-1})
on the top-right corner of (
3.15
) is a cusp label representative in
\mathcal{CLR}(G_{0},X_{0})
and that
K_{\Psi^{\prime}}=\gamma P_{\Phi_{0}}\gamma^{-1}(\mathbb{A}_{f})\cap{}^{\gamma g^{\ddagger}g\gamma^{-1}}{K_{0}}
. So
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Psi^{\prime}}}
is the boundary mixed Shimura variety associated with
\Psi^{\prime}
and
(G_{0},X_{0},K_{0})
.

By
[
50
, Prop. 4.3.5]
, the bottom arrow of the diagram (
3.15
) above extends to
\mathrm{int}(\tilde{\gamma}_{1}^{-1})\circ g:\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi^{F},p}}\to\mathcal{S}_{K_{(\Phi^{F})^{\prime},p}}
. Since there is an equivariant
(P_{\Phi^{F}}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})\to P_{(\Phi^{F})^{\prime}}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p}))
-action on this map, we have a map
\mathrm{int}(\tilde{\gamma}_{1}^{-1})\circ g:\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi^{F}}}\to\mathcal{S}_{K_{(\Phi^{F})^{\prime}}}
. This ends Construction
3.26
.
\square

Combining the construction above with
[
43
]
and
[
45
, Sec. 2 and Sec. 3]
, we obtain the following statement:

Lemma 3.27
.

The bottom arrow in (
3.15
) can be viewed as a collection of morphisms between boundary mixed Shimura varieties induced by a quasi-isogeny twist in the sense of
[
45
, Sec. 2]
.
Let
\mathcal{Q}_{\Phi^{F},\eta}
(resp.
\mathcal{Q}_{(\Phi^{F})^{\prime},\eta}
) be the universal
1
-motive on
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi^{F}}}
(resp.
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{(\Phi^{F})^{\prime}}}
); let
\mathcal{Q}_{\Phi^{F}}
(resp.
\mathcal{Q}_{(\Phi^{F})^{\prime}}
) be its extension over
\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi^{F}}}
(resp.
\mathcal{S}_{K_{(\Phi^{F})^{\prime}}}
), which is unique if it exists by Lemma
3.18
. We then have
\mathbb{Q}^{\times}
-isogenies between

\mathcal{Q}_{\Phi^{F}}
and (the pullback to
\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi^{F}}}
of)
\mathcal{Q}_{(\Phi^{F})^{\prime}}
, and between the corresponding components
\underline{X}_{?}
,
\underline{Y}_{?}
,
A_{?}
,
A^{\vee}_{?}
,
\mathcal{G}^{\natural}_{?}
,
\mathcal{G}^{\natural,\vee}_{?}
,
T_{?}
and
T^{\vee}_{?}
of
?=\mathcal{Q}_{\Phi^{F}}
and
\mathcal{Q}_{(\Phi^{F})^{\prime}}
.

Furthermore, for any integer
n\geq 1
, this bottom arrow naturally induces
isomorphisms
over
\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi^{F}}}
:

\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}(\frac{1}{n}X_{\mathcal{Q}_{\Phi^{F}}},A_{\mathcal{Q}_{\Phi^{F}}}^{\vee})\cong\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}(\frac{1}{n}X_{\mathcal{Q}_{(\Phi^{F})^{\prime}}},A_{\mathcal{Q}_{(\Phi^{F})^{\prime}}}^{\vee}),

\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}(\frac{1}{n}Y_{\mathcal{Q}_{\Phi^{F}}},A_{\mathcal{Q}_{\Phi^{F}}})\cong\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}(\frac{1}{n}Y_{\mathcal{Q}_{(\Phi^{F})^{\prime}}},A_{\mathcal{Q}_{(\Phi^{F})^{\prime}}}),

\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}(\frac{1}{n}X_{\mathcal{Q}_{\Phi^{F}}},\mathcal{G}^{\natural,\vee}_{\mathcal{Q}_{\Phi^{F}}})\cong\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}(\frac{1}{n}X_{\mathcal{Q}_{(\Phi^{F})^{\prime}}},\mathcal{G}^{\natural,\vee}_{\mathcal{Q}_{(\Phi^{F})^{\prime}}}),

\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}(\frac{1}{n}Y_{\mathcal{Q}_{\Phi^{F}}},\mathcal{G}^{\natural}_{\mathcal{Q}_{\Phi^{F}}})\cong\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}(\frac{1}{n}Y_{\mathcal{Q}_{(\Phi^{F})^{\prime}}},\mathcal{G}^{\natural}_{\mathcal{Q}_{(\Phi^{F})^{\prime}}}),

\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}^{\mathrm{symm}}(\frac{1}{n}X_{\mathcal{Q}_{\Phi^{F}}},\mathcal{G}^{\natural,\vee}_{\mathcal{Q}_{\Phi^{F}}})\cong\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}^{\mathrm{symm}}(\frac{1}{n}X_{\mathcal{Q}_{(\Phi^{F})^{\prime}}},\mathcal{G}^{\natural,\vee}_{\mathcal{Q}_{(\Phi^{F})^{\prime}}}),

and

\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}^{\mathrm{symm}}(\frac{1}{n}Y_{\mathcal{Q}_{\Phi^{F}}},\mathcal{G}^{\natural}_{\mathcal{Q}_{\Phi^{F}}})\cong\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}^{\mathrm{symm}}(\frac{1}{n}Y_{\mathcal{Q}_{(\Phi^{F})^{\prime}}},\mathcal{G}^{\natural}_{\mathcal{Q}_{(\Phi^{F})^{\prime}}}).

The objects on the right-hand sides are viewed as objects over
\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi^{F}}}
by pulling back via
\mathrm{int}(\tilde{\gamma}_{1}^{-1})\circ g
.

Moreover, the isomorphisms above can be pulled back to
\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{0}}}
.

Proof.

The situation satisfies the assumptions in
[
45
, Sec. 2]
to which
[
45
, Sec. 3]
applies. In fact, the isogeny twists are induced by the difference of two symplectic lattices. In our case, they are
V_{\mathbb{Z}}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{F}
and the pullback to
V_{\mathbb{A}_{f}}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{F}
of
\mathbf{V}_{\mathbb{Z}}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{F}
. Then we have the first paragraph. For the second paragraph, the best way to see this is to use the description of the tower
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi^{F}}}\to\overline{\mathrm{Sh}}_{K_{\Phi^{F}}}\to\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi^{F}},h}
at principal level
n
in the characteristic zero theory (see, e.g.,
[
43
, Lem. 3.5.5, Lem. 3.6.3, and Lem. 3.6.8]
) and the fact that
\mathrm{int}(\tilde{\gamma}_{1}^{-1})\circ g
(or rather
\mathrm{int}(\tilde{\gamma}_{1}^{-1})
) induces an isomorphism between
D_{\Phi^{F}}
and
D_{(\Phi^{F})^{\prime}}
and an isomorphism between
P_{\Phi^{F}}(\mathbb{R})U_{\Phi^{F}}(\mathbb{C})
and
P_{(\Phi^{F})^{\prime}}(\mathbb{R})U_{(\Phi^{F})^{\prime}}(\mathbb{C})
. Then the lemma follows from Lemma
3.18
.
∎

4.
Toroidal compactifications of integral models of abelian type

In this section, our main goal is to construct good toroidal compactifications of integral models of
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{2}}(G_{2},X_{2})
, given some open compact subgroup
K_{2,p}
of
G_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
and some neat open compact
K_{2}^{p}
. First, we generalize Deligne’s induction method in
[
20
]
to boundary mixed Shimura varieties of abelian type (see §
4.1
). Then, in Cases (HS), (
\mathrm{STB}_{n}
) and (DL), we construct toroidal compactifications for
\mathrm{Sh}_{K}(G,X_{b})
(see §
4.2
). Finally, we complete the proof of the main theorem in §
4.3
.

4.1.
Deligne’s induction for boundary components

4.1.1.

Let us recall some group-theoretic definitions following
[
20
, 2.0.1]
and
[
36
, 3.3.1]
.
Let
H
be a group. Suppose that there is an action of
\Delta
on
H
, that is, a homomorphism
\Delta\to\mathrm{Aut}H
. Suppose that
\Gamma\subset H
is a
\Delta
-stable subgroup. Suppose that there is a
\Delta
-equivariant map
\varphi:\Gamma\to\Delta
, where
\Delta
acts on itself by inner automorphisms. Suppose that, for any
\gamma\in\Gamma
, the action of
\varphi(\gamma)\in\Delta
on
H
is the same as the inner automorphism of
\gamma
on
H
.

Then we denote
H*_{\Gamma}\Delta:=H\rtimes\Delta/(\gamma,\varphi(\gamma)^{-1})_{\gamma\in\Gamma}
; the group structure of
H*_{\Gamma}\Delta
is inherited from that of
H\rtimes\Delta
.

There is a well-defined natural projection
H*_{\Gamma}\Delta\to\Gamma\backslash H
, sending any representative
(h,d)\in H\times\Delta
of an element in
H*_{\Gamma}\Delta
to
\Gamma\backslash H.

The following lemma is immediate from the definition above:

Lemma 4.1
.

We have an isomorphism of cosets
\Gamma*_{\Gamma}\Delta\backslash H*_{\Gamma}\Delta\cong\Gamma\backslash H
via the well-defined natural projection
H*_{\Gamma}\Delta\to\Gamma\backslash H
. Let
H_{1}
be a
\Delta
-stable subgroup of
H
containing
\Gamma
. Then there is an isomorphism of cosets
H_{1}*_{\Gamma}\Delta\backslash H*_{\Gamma}\Delta\cong H_{1}\backslash H
.

4.1.2.

Let us recall the general setup of the so-called Deligne’s induction following
[
20
, Sec. 2.7]
and
[
36
, 3.3.5]
.
Let
O
be a base scheme. Let
\Gamma
be a locally profinite group. Let
\{S_{\mathcal{H}}\}_{\mathcal{H}}
be an inverse system of quasi-projective
O
-schemes
S_{\mathcal{H}}
labeled by a cofinal collection of open compact subgroups
\mathcal{H}\subset\Gamma
. Let
S:=\varprojlim_{\mathcal{H}}S_{\mathcal{H}}
be the inverse limit.

Suppose that there is a right action of
\Gamma
given by
\gamma_{\mathcal{H}}:S_{\mathcal{H}}\xrightarrow{\sim}S_{\gamma^{-1}\mathcal{H}\gamma}
. Assume that

•

\gamma_{\mathcal{H}}=\mathrm{id}
if
\gamma\in\mathcal{H}
.

•

For any pair of open compact subgroups
\mathcal{H}_{1}\subset\mathcal{H}\subset\Gamma
such that
\mathcal{H}_{1}
is a normal subgroup of
\mathcal{H}
, the quotient
S_{\mathcal{H}_{1}}/(\mathcal{H}/\mathcal{H}_{1})
given by the action of
\gamma_{\mathcal{H}_{1}}
for
\gamma\in\mathcal{H}
induces an isomorphism
S_{\mathcal{H}_{1}}/(\mathcal{H}/\mathcal{H}_{1})\xrightarrow{\sim}S_{\mathcal{H}}
; the transition map
S_{\mathcal{H}_{1}}\to S_{\mathcal{H}}
determined by this action is finite and surjective. This implies that the limit
S
exists as an
O
-scheme.

We call such an action of
\Gamma
a
continuous (right)
\Gamma
-action on
S
. One can also similarly define continuous left actions of
\Gamma
by assigning to
\gamma\in\Gamma
the inverse
\gamma^{-1}_{\mathcal{H}}
for any
\mathcal{H}
above.

Let
f:\Gamma\to\Gamma^{\prime}
be a continuous map of locally profinite groups with a compact kernel
\Delta(\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime})
. Define
Deligne’s induction functor
as
\mathrm{Ind}_{\Gamma}^{\Gamma^{\prime}}S:=S\times\Gamma^{\prime}/\Gamma
, where the action of
\Gamma
on
S\times\Gamma^{\prime}
is defined by
(s,\gamma^{\prime})\cdot\gamma=(s\cdot\gamma,\gamma^{-1}\gamma^{\prime})
. For any compact (but
not necessarily open
) subgroup
\mathcal{H}^{\prime}\subset\Gamma^{\prime}
,

(4.1)

\begin{split}&\mathrm{Ind}_{\Gamma}^{\Gamma^{\prime}}S/\mathcal{H}^{\prime}\\
&\cong(S\times\Gamma^{\prime}/\mathcal{H}^{\prime})/\Gamma\\
&\cong\coprod_{j\in J}[S\times(f(\Gamma)j\mathcal{H}^{\prime}/\mathcal{H}^{\prime})]/\Gamma\\
&\cong\coprod_{j\in J}S/\ker(\Gamma\to\Gamma^{\prime}/j\mathcal{H}^{\prime}j^{-1}),\end{split}

where
j
runs over a complete set of representatives in
\Gamma^{\prime}
of
J:=f(\Gamma)\backslash\Gamma^{\prime}/\mathcal{H}^{\prime}
.

Let
\widehat{\pi}
be a profinite set such that
\Gamma^{\prime}
acts on
\widehat{\pi}
continuously and transitively. Let
\Gamma_{e}^{\prime}
be the stabilizer of
e\in\widehat{\pi}
such that
\Gamma_{e}^{\prime}\backslash\Gamma^{\prime}\xrightarrow{\sim}\widehat{\pi}
is a homeomorphism. Suppose that the map
\Gamma\to\Gamma^{\prime}
factors through a surjective map
\Gamma\twoheadrightarrow\Gamma_{e}^{\prime}
. Then
S\times\Gamma^{\prime}/\Gamma\cong(S/\Delta(\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime})\times\Gamma^{\prime})/\Gamma^{\prime}_{e}.
Note that the quotient
S/\Delta(\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime})
exists: Indeed, for any open compact subgroup
\mathcal{H}
of
\Gamma
such that the conjugation of
\Delta(\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime})
stabilizes
\mathcal{H}
, the quotient
S_{\mathcal{H}}/\Delta(\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime})
factors through a finite quotient since
\Delta(\Gamma^{\prime},\Gamma)
is compact.

4.1.3.

Let
(G,X)
be any Shimura datum. Denote
Z:=Z_{G}
and
\mathrm{Sh}(G,X):=\varprojlim_{K\text{ open compact}}\mathrm{Sh}_{K}(G,X)
. Let

\mathscr{A}(G):=\frac{G(\mathbb{A}_{f})}{Z(\mathbb{Q})^{\overline{\ }}}*_{G(\mathbb{Q})_{+}/Z(\mathbb{Q})}G^{\mathrm{ad}}(\mathbb{Q})^{+}\cong\frac{G(\mathbb{A}_{f})}{Z(\mathbb{Q})^{\overline{\ }}}*_{G(\mathbb{Q})/Z(\mathbb{Q})}G^{\mathrm{ad}}(\mathbb{Q})_{1}.

Let

\mathscr{A}^{\circ}(G):=\frac{G(\mathbb{Q})_{+}^{\overline{\ }}}{Z(\mathbb{Q})^{\overline{\ }}}*_{G(\mathbb{Q})_{+}/Z(\mathbb{Q})}G^{\mathrm{ad}}(\mathbb{Q})^{+}.

The “
{}^{\overline{\ }}
” denotes the closure of corresponding groups of
\mathbb{Q}
-points in
G(\mathbb{A}_{f})
.
By
[
20
, 2.1.13-2.1.15]
,
\mathscr{A}(G)
acts transitively on
\widehat{\pi}_{0}(G):=\varprojlim_{K}\pi_{0}(\mathrm{Sh}_{K}(G,X)_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}})\cong G(\mathbb{A}_{f})/G(\mathbb{Q})_{+}^{\overline{\ }}
. The group
\widehat{\pi}_{0}(G)
is an abelian group. The stabilizer of this action is
\mathscr{A}^{\circ}(G)
.

Let
K
be a neat open compact subgroup of
G(\mathbb{A}_{f})
. For any
g\in G(\mathbb{A}_{f})
and fix a connected component
X^{+}
of
X
, the connected component of
\mathrm{Sh}_{K}(G,X)(\mathbb{C})
represented by
g
is
\Gamma_{g}\backslash X^{+}
, where
\Gamma_{g}:=G(\mathbb{Q})_{+}\cap gKg^{-1}
is a congruence subgroup. Moreover,
\Gamma_{g}
acts on
X^{+}
through its image in
G^{\mathrm{ad}}(\mathbb{Q})^{+}
. When
K
runs over all open compact subgroup of
G(\mathbb{A}_{f})
, the image of
\Gamma_{g}
in
G^{\mathrm{ad}}(\mathbb{Q})^{+}
forms a fundamental system of neighborhoods of the identity element
1\in G^{\mathrm{ad}}(\mathbb{Q})^{+}
, and defines a topology on
G^{\mathrm{ad}}(\mathbb{Q})^{+}
.

The left action of
G^{\mathrm{ad}}(\mathbb{Q})^{+}
on
\mathrm{Sh}^{+}(\mathbb{C}):=\varprojlim_{K}\mathrm{Sh}_{K}^{+,(g)}(\mathbb{C})
, the inverse limit of
\mathrm{Sh}_{K}^{+,(g)}(\mathbb{C}):=\Gamma_{g}\backslash X^{+}
for any
g
, induces an action of the completion
G^{\mathrm{ad}}(\mathbb{Q}){}^{+,\widehat{\ }}
(rel.
G(\mathbb{Q})_{+}
) of
G^{\mathrm{ad}}(\mathbb{Q})^{+}
with respect to the topology defined by congruence subgroups of
G(\mathbb{Q})_{+}
as above. By
[
20
, 2.0.13 and 2.1.15]
,
G^{\mathrm{ad}}(\mathbb{Q}){}^{+,\widehat{\ }}
(rel.
G(\mathbb{Q})_{+}
) is canonically isomorphic to
G^{\mathrm{ad}}(\mathbb{Q}){}^{+,\widehat{\ }}
(rel.
G^{\mathrm{der}}(\mathbb{Q})_{+}
), the completion of
G^{\mathrm{ad}}(\mathbb{Q}){}^{+}
with respect to the topology defined by the congruence subgroups of
G^{\mathrm{der}}(\mathbb{Q})_{+}
. Moreover, we have
\mathscr{A}^{\circ}(G)\cong G^{\mathrm{ad}}(\mathbb{Q}){}^{+,\widehat{\ }}\text{(rel. }G^{\mathrm{der}}(\mathbb{Q})_{+}\text{)}
.

Since
\mathrm{Sh}_{K}^{+,(g)}(\mathbb{C})
admits a canonical algebraization over
\mathbb{C}
denoted by
\mathrm{Sh}_{K,\mathbb{C}}^{+,(g)}
, we denote
\mathrm{Sh}^{+}_{\mathbb{C}}:=\varprojlim_{K}\mathrm{Sh}_{K,\mathbb{C}}^{+,(g)}
, and the action of
\mathscr{A}^{\circ}(G)
canonically algebraizes to an action on
\mathrm{Sh}^{+}_{\mathbb{C}}
. Moreover, the variety
\mathrm{Sh}^{+}_{K,\mathbb{C}}
descends to a variety
\mathrm{Sh}^{+,(g)}_{K,\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}
over
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}
, and the action of
\mathscr{A}^{\circ}(G)
is also defined over
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}
. Denote
\mathrm{Sh}^{+}_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}:=\varprojlim_{K}\mathrm{Sh}_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}^{+,(g)}
.

Hence, we have that (see
[
20
, 2.1.15]
)

\mathrm{Sh}(G,X)_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}\cong(\mathrm{Sh}_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}^{+}\times\mathscr{A}(G))/\mathscr{A}^{\circ}(G).

Let
(G_{2},X_{2})
be a Shimura datum such that there is a central isogeny
G^{\mathrm{der}}\to G^{\mathrm{der}}_{2}
that induces an isomorphism
(G^{\mathrm{ad}},X^{\mathrm{ad}})\cong(G_{2}^{\mathrm{ad}},X_{2}^{\mathrm{ad}})
. As any pullback of a congruence subgroup of
G_{2}^{\mathrm{der}}(\mathbb{Q})
contains a congruence subgroup of
G^{\mathrm{der}}(\mathbb{Q})
, we see that there is a natural continuous surjective map

\mathscr{A}^{\circ}(G)\longrightarrow\mathscr{A}^{\circ}(G_{2}).

Let
\Delta^{\circ}(G,G_{2})
be the kernel of the map above. Following almost the same proof as
[
37
, Lem. E. 6]
, the group
\Delta^{\circ}(G,G_{2})
is compact: To see this, it suffices to replace the Galois cohomology input there with the fact that

\ker(H^{1}(\mathbb{Q},Z_{G})\to\prod H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{l},Z_{G}))
is finite. Hence, the kernel of
\mathscr{A}(G)\to\mathscr{A}(G^{\mathrm{ad}})
contains
Z_{G}(\mathbb{A}_{f})/Z_{G}(\mathbb{Q})^{\overline{\ }}
as a finite index subgroup.

From
[
20
, 2.7.11]
(see also
[
36
, Prop. 3.3.10]
and
4.1.2
), we have that

\mathrm{Sh}(G_{2},X_{2})_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}\cong(\mathrm{Sh}_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}^{+}\times\mathscr{A}(G_{2}))/\mathscr{A}^{\circ}(G).

4.1.4.

Let
(G,X)
be any Shimura datum. Let
\Phi=(Q,X^{+},g)
be any cusp label representative. Let
K
be a neat open compact subgroup of
G(\mathbb{A}_{f})
.

Let
Y_{\Phi}
be any connected normal
\mathbb{Q}
-subgroup of
Q
such that
P_{\Phi}\subset Y_{\Phi}\subset ZP_{\Phi}
. Let
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi}^{Y}}(\mathbb{C}):=\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi}^{Y}}(Y_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f}),Y_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})D_{\Phi})(\mathbb{C})
, where
K_{\Phi}^{Y}:=Y_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f})\cap gKg^{-1}
.

Fix a connected component
D_{\Phi}^{+}\subset D_{\Phi}
, then this choice determines an isomorphism between the group
\pi_{0}(Y_{\Phi})_{K}
of connected components of the mixed Shimura variety
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi}^{Y}}(\mathbb{C})
and
Y_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})_{+}\backslash Y_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f})/K_{\Phi}
, where
Y_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})_{+}:=\mathrm{Stab}_{Y_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})}(D_{\Phi}^{+})
. Then
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi}^{Y}}(\mathbb{C})
is isomorphic to a disjoint union

\coprod_{p_{f}\in Y_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})_{+}\backslash Y_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f})/K_{\Phi}}\Gamma_{p_{f}}\backslash D_{\Phi}^{+}.

In the equation above,
\Gamma_{p_{f}}:=Y_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})_{+}\cap p_{f}K_{\Phi}^{Y}p^{-1}_{f}.

Fix a
p_{f}\in Y_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
. Denote
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi}^{Y}}^{+,(p_{f})}(\mathbb{C}):=\Gamma_{p_{f}}\backslash D_{\Phi}^{+}
. By
[
64
, Ch.9]
,
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi}^{Y}}^{+,(p_{f})}(\mathbb{C})
uniquely algebraizes to an algebraic variety
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi}^{Y},\mathbb{C}}^{+,(p_{f})}
; by
[
64
, Ch.11]
, this algebraic variety descends to an algebraic variety
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi}^{Y},\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}^{+,(p_{f})}
over
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}
. Let
\mathrm{Sh}_{Y_{\Phi},\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}^{+}:=\varprojlim_{K}\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi}^{Y},\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}^{+,(p_{f})}
, which does not depend on
p_{f}
. Denote
\mathrm{Sh}_{Y_{\Phi}}:=\varprojlim_{K\text{ open compact}}\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi}^{Y}}\cong\varprojlim_{K_{\Phi}^{Y}\text{ open compact in }Y_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f})}\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi}^{Y}}
.

We set

\mathscr{A}(Y_{\Phi}):=\frac{Y_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f})}{Z^{Y}_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})^{\overline{\ }}}*_{Y_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})_{+}/Z^{Y}_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})}Y_{\Phi}^{\mathrm{ad}}(\mathbb{Q})^{+},

and set

\mathscr{A}^{\circ}(Y_{\Phi}):=\frac{Y_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})_{+}^{\overline{\ }}}{Z^{Y}_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})^{\overline{\ }}}*_{Y_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})_{+}/Z^{Y}_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})}Y_{\Phi}^{\mathrm{ad}}(\mathbb{Q})^{+}.

In the two equations above,
Z^{Y}_{\Phi}:=Z_{G}\cap Y_{\Phi}
and
Y_{\Phi}^{\mathrm{ad}}:=Y_{\Phi}/Z^{Y}_{\Phi}
. So
P^{\mathrm{ad}}_{\Phi}=Y_{\Phi}^{\mathrm{ad}}=ZP_{\Phi}^{\mathrm{ad}}
. The “
\overline{\ }
” denotes the closure in
Y_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
. The group
\mathscr{A}(Y_{\Phi})
acts on
\widehat{\pi}_{0}(Y_{\Phi}):=\varprojlim\pi_{0}(Y_{\Phi})_{K}
transitively with stabilizer
\mathscr{A}^{\circ}(Y_{\Phi})
.

For any closed connected unipotent normal subgroup
W\subset Y_{\Phi}
, we can also define

\mathscr{A}(Y_{\Phi}/W):=\frac{Y_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f})/W(\mathbb{A}_{f})}{Z^{Y}_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})^{\overline{\ }}}*_{(Y_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})_{+}/W(\mathbb{Q}))/Z^{Y}_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})}Y_{\Phi}^{\mathrm{ad}}(\mathbb{Q})^{+}/W(\mathbb{Q}),

and define

\mathscr{A}^{\circ}(Y_{\Phi}/W):=\frac{Y_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})_{+}^{\overline{\ }}/W(\mathbb{Q})^{\overline{\ }}}{Z^{Y}_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})^{\overline{\ }}}*_{(Y_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})_{+}/W(\mathbb{Q}))/Z^{Y}_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})}Y_{\Phi}^{\mathrm{ad}}(\mathbb{Q})^{+}/W(\mathbb{Q}).

Lemma 4.2
.

With the notation above,

(1)

\mathscr{A}(Y_{\Phi})
and
\mathscr{A}^{\circ}(Y_{\Phi})
fit into the following exact sequences induced by Levi quotients:

(4.2)

\begin{split}0\longrightarrow W_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f})\longrightarrow\mathscr{A}(Y_{\Phi})\longrightarrow\mathscr{A}(Y_{\Phi}/W_{\Phi})\longrightarrow 0,\\
0\longrightarrow W_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f})\longrightarrow\mathscr{A}^{\circ}(Y_{\Phi})\longrightarrow\mathscr{A}^{\circ}(Y_{\Phi}/W_{\Phi})\longrightarrow 0.\end{split}

(2)

\mathscr{A}^{\circ}(Y_{\Phi})
is the completion of
Y_{\Phi}^{\mathrm{ad}}(\mathbb{Q})^{+}
with respect to the topology generated by the images of congruence subgroups of
Y_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})_{+}
in
Y_{\Phi}^{\mathrm{ad}}(\mathbb{Q})^{+}.

(3)

Let
Y^{\prime}_{\Phi}
be the smallest normal
\mathbb{Q}
-subgroup of
Y_{\Phi}
such that there is a product
Y^{\prime}_{\Phi}\cdot Z_{\Phi}^{Y}\cong Y_{\Phi}
and such that
Y_{\Phi}^{\prime}
and
Z^{Y}_{\phi}
have finite intersection. Then the definition of
\mathscr{A}^{\circ}(Y_{\Phi})
depends only on
Y^{\prime}_{\Phi}
.

Proof.

By
[
66
, Lem. 2.7 and Prop. 6.6]
,
W_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A})\cong W_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})^{\overline{\ }}
. Since
W_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f})*_{W_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})}W_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})\cong W_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
, the first part follows. For any open compact subgroup
K\subset Y_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
, by
[
66
, Prop. 6.5, p.296]
, the image of
K
in the quotient
Y_{\Phi}/W_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
is open and compact. Combining this fact with strong approximation of unipotent groups, and choosing a Levi decomposition representing
Y_{\Phi}
as a semidirect product of
W_{\Phi}
and
Y_{\Phi}/W_{\Phi}
, we see that the family of congruence subgroups of
Y_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})
contains a cofinal system consisting of the products of congruence subgroups of
W_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})
and
Y_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})/W_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})
. Then the second part follows from
[
20
, 2.0.9]
and the first part. To show the third part, we can assume that
Y_{\Phi}
is reductive by the first part. Then the third part is the consequence of the following Lemma
4.3
that can be directly derived from
[
20
, 2.0.13]
.
∎

Lemma 4.3
.

Let
Y
be a reductive
\mathbb{Q}
-group. Let
Y_{1}
be a
\mathbb{Q}
-subgroup containing
Y^{\mathrm{der}}
. Let
Z_{1}
be a subgroup of
Z_{Y}
such that
Z_{1}
and
Y_{1}
have finite intersection and such that
Z_{1}\cdot Y_{1}\cong Y
. Then the product of a congruence subgroup in
Y_{1}
and a congruence subgroup in
Z_{1}
is a congruence subgroup of
Y
.

Proof.

By
[
20
, 2.0.13]
, any congruence subgroup
U_{1}\subset Y_{1}(\mathbb{Q})
contains a finite index subgroup
U^{\prime}
, where
U^{\prime}
is a product of a congruence subgroup
U^{\mathrm{der}}
of
Y^{\mathrm{der}}
and a congruence subgroup
U_{Z}^{1}
of
Z^{\circ}_{Y_{1}}(\mathbb{Q})
, which is a finite index subgroup of group of units of
Z^{\circ}_{Y_{1}}(\mathbb{Q})
by
[
20
, 2.0.10]
. By
loc. cit.
again, the product of the congruence subgroup
U_{Z}^{1}
and a congruence subgroup
U_{Z}\subset Z_{1}
is a congruence subgroup in
Z_{1}\cdot Z_{Y_{1}}(\mathbb{Q})\cong Z_{Y}(\mathbb{Q})
. Hence,
U^{\prime}\cdot U_{Z}
is a congruence subgroup of
Y(\mathbb{Q})
by
[
20
, 2.0.13]
again, and so is
U_{1}\cdot U_{Z}
.
∎

4.1.5.

By part 2 of Lemma
4.2
and the paragraphs in §
4.1.4
before that, we have an
\mathscr{A}(Y_{\Phi})
-equivariant isomorphism

\mathrm{Sh}_{Y_{\Phi},\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}\cong\mathrm{Sh}^{+}_{Y_{\Phi},\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}\times\mathscr{A}(Y_{\Phi})/\mathscr{A}^{\circ}(Y_{\Phi}).

Let
E^{\prime}
be a finite extension of
E:=E(G,X)=E(P_{\Phi},D_{\Phi})
(see
[
64
, Prop. 12.1]
).

The action of
\mathrm{Gal}(E^{\prime,ab}/E^{\prime})
on
\widehat{\pi}_{0}(Y_{\Phi})\cong Y_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f})/Y_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})_{+}^{\overline{\ }}\cong(Y_{\Phi}/W_{\Phi})(\mathbb{A}_{f})/(Y_{\Phi}/W_{\Phi})(\mathbb{Q})_{+}^{\overline{\ }}
is given by

(4.3)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces r_{E^{\prime}}(Y_{{\Phi}}):\mathrm{Gal}(E^{\prime,ab}/E^{\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{0}(\mathbb{G}_{m}(\mathbb{A}_{E^{\prime}})/\mathbb{G}_{m}(E^{\prime}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\frac{\mathbb{A}_{E^{\prime}}^{\times}}{E^{\prime,\times}(E^{\prime}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{R})^{\times,+}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong\mathbb{A}_{E^{\prime},f}^{\times}/E^{\prime,\times,+}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{E^{\prime},f})/Y_{\Phi}(E^{\prime})_{+}^{\overline{\ }}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\frac{Y_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f})}{Y_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})_{+}^{\overline{\ }}}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{rec_{E^{\prime}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces[\mu_{{\Phi},x}]}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N_{E^{\prime}/\mathbb{Q}}}

In the diagram (
4.3
) above,
[\mu_{\Phi,x}]
is the morphism determined by the conjugacy class of Hodge cocharacter
\mu_{\Phi,x}
for any
x\in Y_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})D_{\Phi}
over
E^{\prime}
;
N_{E^{\prime}/\mathbb{Q}}
is the map determined by norm map
N_{E^{\prime}/\mathbb{Q}}:R_{E^{\prime}/\mathbb{Q}}Y_{\Phi,E^{\prime}}\to Y_{\Phi}
.
Let
\widetilde{r}_{E^{\prime}}(Y_{\Phi})
be the composition of
r_{E^{\prime}}(Y_{\Phi})
with the natural map
\mathrm{Gal}(\overline{E}^{\prime}/E^{\prime})\to\mathrm{Gal}(E^{\prime,ab}/E^{\prime})
.

Let
\mathscr{E}(Y_{\Phi})
be the pullback of

0\longrightarrow\mathscr{A}^{\circ}(Y_{\Phi})\longrightarrow\mathscr{A}(Y_{\Phi})\longrightarrow\mathscr{A}^{\circ}(Y_{\Phi})\backslash\mathscr{A}(Y_{\Phi})\longrightarrow 0

via
-\widetilde{r}_{E^{\prime}}(Y_{\Phi})
. Then
\mathscr{E}(Y_{\Phi})
is the stabilizer of
\mathrm{Sh}_{Y_{\Phi},\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}^{+}
in
\mathscr{A}(Y_{\Phi})\times\mathrm{Gal}(\overline{E}^{\prime}/E^{\prime})
.

So there is an action of
\mathscr{A}(Y_{\Phi})\times\mathrm{Gal}(\overline{E}^{\prime}/E^{\prime})
on
\mathrm{Sh}_{Y_{\Phi},\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}
such that the natural projection
\widehat{\pi}_{0}:\mathrm{Sh}_{Y_{\Phi},\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}\to\widehat{\pi}_{0}(Y_{\Phi})
from
\mathrm{Sh}_{Y_{\Phi},\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}
to its group of connected components is
\mathscr{A}(Y_{\Phi})\times\mathrm{Gal}(\overline{E}^{\prime}/E^{\prime})
-equivariant.

4.1.6.

Let
\Phi=(Q,X^{+},g)
be a cusp label representative of
(G,X)
. The cusp label representative
\Phi
maps to a cusp label representative
\Phi^{\mathrm{ad}}=(Q^{\mathrm{ad}},X^{\mathrm{ad},+},g)
of
(G^{\mathrm{ad}},X^{\mathrm{ad}})
, where
Q^{\mathrm{ad}}=Q/Z_{G}
,
X^{\mathrm{ad},+}
is the image of
X^{+}
and
g
here abusively denotes the image of
g
.

Lemma 4.4
.

The map
D_{Q,X^{+}}\to D_{Q^{\mathrm{ad}},X^{\mathrm{ad},+}}
induced by
\Phi\to\Phi^{\mathrm{ad}}
is injective and is a homeomorphism on each connected component of
D_{Q,X^{+}}
.

Proof.

We have a
P_{Q}(\mathbb{R})\cdot U_{Q}(\mathbb{C})
-equivariant diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{0}(X)\times\mathrm{Hom}(\mathbb{S}_{\mathbb{C}},P_{Q,\mathbb{C}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\mathrm{ad}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{0}(X^{\mathrm{ad}})\times\mathrm{Hom}(\mathbb{S}_{\mathbb{C}},P_{Q^{\mathrm{ad}},\mathbb{C}}).}

By
[
64
, Prop. 2.9]
, we can write
D_{Q,X^{+}}
as a quotient
P_{Q}(\mathbb{R})U_{Q}(\mathbb{C})/\mathrm{Stab}(x)
where
\mathrm{Stab}(x)
is the stabilizer of a point in
D_{Q,X^{+}}
. The map
D_{Q,X^{+}}\to D_{Q^{\mathrm{ad}},X^{\mathrm{ad},+}}
is induced by the quotient of
P_{Q}(\mathbb{R})U_{Q}(\mathbb{C})
by
(P_{Q}\cap Z_{G})(\mathbb{R})
, and the latter group is contained in
\mathrm{Stab}(x)
.
∎

Let
(G_{2},X_{2})
be the Shimura datum as in §
4.1.3
.
Let
\Phi
(resp.
\Phi_{2}
) be a cusp label representative of
(G,X)
(resp.
(G_{2},X_{2})
). Recall that
Y^{\prime}_{\Phi}
is the smallest normal
\mathbb{Q}
-subgroup of
Y_{\Phi}
such that
Y^{\prime}_{\Phi}
and
Z^{Y}_{\Phi}
have finite intersection and such that
Y^{\prime}_{\Phi}\cdot Z_{\Phi}^{Y}\cong Y_{\Phi}
.

We assume that there is an isogeny
Y^{\prime}_{\Phi}\to Y^{\prime}_{\Phi_{2}}
whose kernel is in
Z_{G}\cap Y^{\prime}_{\Phi}
.

From Part 2 and Part 3 of Lemma
4.2
, we see that there is a natural continuous and surjective map

\mathscr{A}^{\circ}(P_{\Phi})\to\mathscr{A}^{\circ}(Y_{\Phi_{2}}).

Lemma 4.5
.

The kernel
\Delta^{\circ}(P_{\Phi},Y_{\Phi_{2}}):=\ker(\mathscr{A}^{\circ}(P_{\Phi})\to\mathscr{A}^{\circ}(Y_{\Phi_{2}}))
is compact. The intersection
(Z_{G}\cap P_{\Phi})(\mathbb{A}_{f})/(Z_{G}\cap P_{\Phi})(\mathbb{Q})^{\overline{\ }}\cap\Delta^{\circ}(P_{\Phi},Y_{\Phi_{2}})
is a finite index subgroup in
\Delta^{\circ}(P_{\Phi},Y_{\Phi_{2}})
.

Proof.

Combine Part 1 of Lemma
4.2
and
[
37
, Lem. E. 6]
, with the Galois cohomology input there changed to the fact that

\ker(H^{1}(\mathbb{Q},Z_{G}\cap P_{\Phi})\longrightarrow\prod H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{l},Z_{G}\cap P_{\Phi}))
is finite.
∎

Note that
\mathrm{Sh}_{Y_{\Phi_{2}},\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}
is equipped with a Hecke action of
Y_{\Phi_{2}}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
on the right (see
[
64
, 11.5]
). From the arguments above, we see that

Proposition 4.6
.

There is an
\mathscr{A}(Y_{\Phi_{2}})
-equivariant isomorphism

\mathrm{Sh}_{Y_{\Phi_{2}},\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}\cong\mathrm{Sh}^{+}_{P_{\Phi},\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}\times\mathscr{A}(Y_{\Phi_{2}})/\mathscr{A}^{\circ}(P_{\Phi}).

The
\mathscr{A}(Y_{\Phi_{2}})
-action of the right-hand side is induced by the right multiplication on its second factor.

From the Proposition above, we see that
\mathscr{E}(Y_{\Phi_{2}})
is the pushout of
0\to\mathscr{A}^{\circ}(P_{\Phi})\to\mathscr{E}(P_{\Phi})\to\mathrm{Gal}(\overline{E}^{\prime}/E^{\prime})\to 0
under the natural map
\mathscr{A}^{\circ}(P_{\Phi})\to\mathscr{A}^{\circ}(Y_{\Phi_{2}})
given by Part 3 of Lemma
4.2
.
So
\mathscr{A}(Y_{\Phi_{2}})*_{\mathscr{A}^{\circ}(P_{\Phi})}\mathscr{E}(P_{\Phi})\cong\mathscr{A}(Y_{\Phi_{2}})\times\mathrm{Gal}(\overline{E}^{\prime}/E^{\prime})
.

So there is an action of
\mathscr{A}(Y_{\Phi_{2}})\times\mathrm{Gal}(\overline{E}^{\prime}/E^{\prime})
on
\mathrm{Sh}_{Y_{\Phi_{2}},\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}
such that the natural projection
\widehat{\pi}_{0}:\mathrm{Sh}_{Y_{\Phi_{2}},\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}\to\widehat{\pi}_{0}(Y_{\Phi_{2}})
from
\mathrm{Sh}_{Y_{\Phi_{2}},\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}
to its group of connected components is
\mathscr{A}(Y_{\Phi_{2}})\times\mathrm{Gal}(\overline{E}^{\prime}/E^{\prime})
-equivariant.

Recall that any
g_{2}\in G_{2}(\mathbb{Q})
induces an isomorphism
\mathrm{Sh}_{Y_{\Phi_{2}},\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathrm{Sh}_{{}^{g_{2}}{Y_{\Phi_{2}}},\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}
, where
\mathrm{Sh}_{{}^{g_{2}}{Y_{\Phi_{2}}},\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}
is the inverse limit of a tower of mixed Shimura varieties
\mathrm{Sh}_{{}^{g_{2}}{K^{Y}_{\Phi_{2}}}}({}^{g_{2}}{Y_{\Phi_{2}}}(\mathbb{A}_{f}),{}^{g_{2}}{Y_{\Phi_{2}}(\mathbb{Q})}g_{2}\cdot D_{\Phi_{2}})_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}
. Also, there is a right action of
h_{2}\in G_{2}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
, mapping
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi_{2}}^{Y}}(Y_{\Phi_{2}},Y_{\Phi_{2}}(\mathbb{Q})D_{\Phi_{2}})_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}
to
\mathrm{Sh}_{{(K_{\Phi_{2}}^{Y})}^{h_{2}}}(Y_{\Phi_{2}},Y_{\Phi_{2}}(\mathbb{Q})D_{\Phi_{2}})_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}
. Therefore, there is a right action
h_{2}:\mathrm{Sh}_{Y_{\Phi_{2}},\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathrm{Sh}_{Y_{\Phi_{2}},\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}
on the inverse limit
\mathrm{Sh}_{Y_{\Phi_{2}},\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}
.

4.1.7.

As in
[
36
]
and
[
33
]
, we want to decompose
\mathscr{A}(Y_{\Phi_{2}})
to smaller groups.

Let us define more “
\mathscr{A}
-type” and “
\mathscr{A}^{\circ}
-type” groups.

Fix any general
(G,X)
and
Y_{\Phi}
. Let
K:=K_{p}K^{p}
be a neat open compact subgroup of
G(\mathbb{A}_{f})
such that
K^{p}\subset G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
is neat open compact. We define

\widetilde{\mathscr{A}}_{K_{p}}(Y_{\Phi}):=\frac{Y_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})\times K_{\Phi,p}^{Y}}{Z_{\Phi,K_{p}}^{Y}({\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}})^{\overline{\ }}}*_{Y_{\Phi,K_{p}}({\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}})_{+}/Z_{\Phi,K_{p}}^{Y}({\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}})}P_{\Phi,K_{p}}^{\mathrm{ad}}({\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}})^{+}.

In the equation above,
Y_{\Phi,K_{p}}({\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}})_{+}:=Y_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})_{+}\cap\widetilde{K}_{\Phi,p}^{Y}
,
Z_{\Phi,K_{p}}^{Y}({\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}):=Z_{G}(\mathbb{Q})\cap K_{\Phi,p}^{Y}
, and
P^{\mathrm{ad}}_{\Phi,K_{p}}({\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}})^{+}:=(Y_{\Phi}^{\mathrm{ad}})(\mathbb{Q})^{+}\cap\im K_{\Phi,p}^{Y}=(ZP_{\Phi}/Z_{G})(\mathbb{Q})^{+}\cap\im K_{\Phi,p}^{Y}
, where
\im K_{\Phi,p}^{Y}
is the image of
K_{\Phi,p}^{Y}
in
G^{\mathrm{ad}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
.

We define

\widetilde{\mathscr{A}}^{\circ}_{K_{p}}(Y_{\Phi}):=\frac{Y_{\Phi,K_{p}}({\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}})_{+}^{\overline{\ }}}{Z^{Y}_{\Phi,K_{p}}({\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}})^{\overline{\ }}}*_{Y_{\Phi,K_{p}}({\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}})_{+}/Z^{Y}_{\Phi,K_{p}}({\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}})}P_{\Phi,K_{p}}^{\mathrm{ad}}({\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}})^{+}.

Therefore,
\widetilde{\mathscr{A}}^{\circ}(Y_{\Phi})
is the closure of
P_{\Phi,K_{p}}^{\mathrm{ad}}({\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}})^{+}
in
\mathscr{A}^{\circ}(Y_{\Phi})
(with respect to the topology on
\mathscr{A}^{\circ}(Y_{\Phi})
defined in §
4.1.4
).
Moreover, let

\mathscr{A}^{\circ}_{K_{p}}(Y_{\Phi}):=\frac{Y_{\Phi,K_{p}}({\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}})_{+}^{\overline{p}}}{Z^{Y}_{\Phi,K_{p}}({\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}})^{\overline{p}}}*_{Y_{\Phi,K_{p}}({\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}})_{+}/Z^{Y}_{\Phi,K_{p}}({\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}})}P_{\Phi,K_{p}}^{\mathrm{ad}}({\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}})^{+}.

In the definition above, “
\overline{p}
” denotes the closure in
Y_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
.
Then
\mathscr{A}^{\circ}_{K_{p}}(Y_{\Phi})
is the completion of
P_{K_{p}}^{\mathrm{ad}}({\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}})^{+}
with respect to the topology generated by the image of the sets of the form
Y^{\prime}_{K_{p}}({\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}})_{+}\cap K^{p}
in
P_{\Phi}^{\mathrm{ad}}(\mathbb{Q})^{+}
by Lemma
4.2
and
[
20
, 2.0.12]
.

Define

\mathscr{A}_{K_{p}}(Y_{\Phi}):=\frac{Y_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})}{Z^{Y}_{\Phi,K_{p}}({\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}})^{\overline{p}}}*_{Y_{\Phi,K_{p}}({\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}})_{+}/Z^{Y}_{\Phi,K_{p}}({\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}})}P_{\Phi,K_{p}}^{\mathrm{ad}}({\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}})^{+}.

Now, let
(G,X)
,
(G_{2},X_{2})
,
\Phi
,
\Phi_{2}
,
P_{\Phi}
and
Y_{\Phi_{2}}
be the symbols defined as in §
4.1.6
. Let
K_{2}=K_{2,p}K^{p}_{2}
be a neat open compact subgroup of
G_{2}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
with
K_{2}^{p}
neat open compact. So we have defined
\widetilde{\mathscr{A}}^{\circ}_{K_{2,p}}(Y_{\Phi_{2}})
and
\mathscr{A}^{\circ}_{K_{2,p}}(Y_{\Phi_{2}})
as above.

Define
\widetilde{\mathscr{A}}^{\circ}_{K_{2,p}}(P_{\Phi})
as the closure of
P^{\mathrm{ad}}_{\Phi_{2},K_{2,p}}({\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}})^{+}
in
\mathscr{A}^{\circ}(P_{\Phi})
.
Since there is a central isogeny
P^{\prime}_{\Phi}\to Y^{\prime}_{\Phi_{2}}
, we can choose a neat open compact subgroup
K_{p}\subset G(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
such that
g_{\Phi}K_{p}g_{\Phi}^{-1}\cap P^{\prime}_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
is a normal subgroup contained in the pullback of
g_{\Phi_{2}}K_{2,p}g_{\Phi_{2}}^{-1}\cap Y^{\prime}_{\Phi_{2}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
under this central isogeny.

Define
\mathscr{A}^{\circ}_{K_{2,p}}(P_{\Phi})
to be the completion of
P^{\mathrm{ad}}_{\Phi_{2},K_{2,p}}({\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}})^{+}
with respect to the topology
generated by the image of the sets of the form
P^{\prime}_{K_{p}}({\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}})_{+}\cap K^{p}
in
P_{\Phi_{2}}^{\mathrm{ad}}(\mathbb{Q})^{+}
.

Explicitly, we can write

\widetilde{\mathscr{A}}^{\circ}_{K_{2,p}}(P_{\Phi}):=\frac{P_{\Phi,K_{p}}({\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}})_{+}^{\overline{\ }}}{Z_{\Phi,K_{p}}({\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}})^{\overline{\ }}}*_{P_{\Phi,K_{p}}({\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}})_{+}/Z_{\Phi,K_{p}}({\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}})}P_{\Phi_{2},K_{2,p}}^{\mathrm{ad}}({\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}})^{+},

and write

\mathscr{A}^{\circ}_{K_{2,p}}(P_{\Phi}):=\frac{P_{\Phi,K_{p}}({\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}})_{+}^{\overline{p}}}{Z_{\Phi,K_{p}}({\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}})^{\overline{p}}}*_{P_{\Phi,K_{p}}({\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}})_{+}/Z_{\Phi,K_{p}}({\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}})}P_{\Phi_{2},K_{2,p}}^{\mathrm{ad}}({\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}})^{+}.

The maps
\widetilde{\mathscr{A}}^{\circ}_{K_{2,p}}(P_{\Phi})\to\widetilde{\mathscr{A}}^{\circ}_{K_{2,p}}(Y_{\Phi_{2}})
and
\mathscr{A}^{\circ}_{K_{2,p}}(P_{\Phi})\to\mathscr{A}^{\circ}_{K_{2,p}}(Y_{\Phi_{2}})
are surjective; their kernels are compact by Lemma
4.5
.

4.1.8.

For a general
(G,X)
,
define

\mathscr{J}_{K^{Y}_{\Phi,p}}(Y_{\Phi}):=Y_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})_{+}\backslash Y_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})/K_{\Phi,p}^{Y}.

We simplify this notation to
\mathscr{J}_{K_{p}}(Y_{\Phi})
if we know how to form the group
K^{Y}_{\Phi,p}
from some
K_{p}\subset G(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
.
Then
\mathscr{J}_{K_{p}}(Y_{\Phi})\cong(Y_{\Phi}/W_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})_{+})\backslash(Y_{\Phi}/W_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q}_{p}))/K_{\Phi,h,p}^{Y}
is an abelian group by
[
20
, 2.1.3]
, where
K_{\Phi,h,p}^{Y}
is the image of
K^{Y}_{\Phi,p}
in
Y_{\Phi}/W_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
. If we assume that
K_{p}
is hyperspecial,
K^{Y}_{\Phi,h,p}
is also hyperspecial. By
[
36
, Lem. 2.2.6]
,
\mathscr{J}_{K_{p}}(Y_{\Phi})
is trivial.

Lemma 4.7
.

With the previous definitions, there is a natural injective map

\widetilde{\mathscr{A}}^{\circ}_{K_{p}}(Y_{\Phi})\backslash\widetilde{\mathscr{A}}_{K_{p}}(Y_{\Phi})/K^{Y}_{\Phi,p}\hookrightarrow\mathscr{A}^{\circ}(Y_{\Phi})\backslash\mathscr{A}(Y_{\Phi})/K^{Y}_{\Phi,p},

where
\widetilde{\mathscr{A}}^{\circ}_{K_{p}}(Y_{\Phi})\backslash\widetilde{\mathscr{A}}_{K_{p}}(Y_{\Phi})/K^{Y}_{\Phi,p}\cong\mathscr{A}^{\circ}_{K_{p}}(Y_{\Phi})\backslash\mathscr{A}_{K_{p}}(Y_{\Phi})
. The index of the left-hand side in the right-hand side is isomorphic to
\mathscr{J}_{K_{p}}(Y_{\Phi})
.

Therefore, we can write
\mathscr{A}(Y_{\Phi})
as a fiber bundle over
\mathscr{J}_{K_{p}}(Y_{\Phi})
; at each
j\in\mathscr{J}_{K_{p}}(Y_{\Phi})
, the fiber is
{\mathscr{A}^{\circ}_{{}^{j}{K_{p}}}(Y_{\Phi})}\backslash{\mathscr{A}_{{}^{j}{K_{p}}}(Y_{\Phi})}\cong\mathscr{A}^{\circ}_{K_{p}}(Y_{\Phi})\backslash\mathscr{A}_{K_{p}}(Y_{\Phi}).
When
K_{p}
is hyperspecial, we have that
\mathscr{A}^{\circ}_{K_{p}}(Y_{\Phi})\backslash\mathscr{A}_{K_{p}}(Y_{\Phi})\cong\mathscr{A}^{\circ}(Y_{\Phi})\backslash\mathscr{A}(Y_{\Phi})/K^{Y}_{\Phi,p}
.

Proof.

By Lemma
4.1
,
\mathscr{A}^{\circ}(Y_{\Phi})\backslash\mathscr{A}(Y_{\Phi})\cong Y_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})_{+}^{\overline{\ }}\backslash Y_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
,
\widetilde{\mathscr{A}}^{\circ}_{K_{p}}(Y_{\Phi})\backslash\widetilde{\mathscr{A}}_{K_{p}}(Y_{\Phi})\cong Y_{\Phi,K_{p}}({\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}})_{+}^{\overline{\ }}\backslash Y_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})\times K_{\Phi,p}^{Y}
and
\mathscr{A}^{\circ}_{K_{p}}(Y_{\Phi})\backslash\mathscr{A}_{K_{p}}(Y_{\Phi})\cong Y_{\Phi,K_{p}}({\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}})_{+}^{\overline{p}}\backslash Y_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
.
Moreover,

\begin{split}&Y_{\Phi,K_{p}}({\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}})_{+}^{\overline{p}}\backslash Y_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})\\
&\cong\varprojlim_{K^{p}}Y_{\Phi,K_{p}}({\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}})_{+}\backslash Y_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})/K^{Y,p}_{\Phi}\\
&\cong\varprojlim_{K^{p}}Y_{\Phi,K_{p}}({\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}})_{+}\backslash Y_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})\times K^{Y}_{\Phi,p}/K^{Y}_{\Phi,p}K^{Y,p}_{\Phi}\\
&\cong\varprojlim_{K_{p}^{\prime}K^{p},\text{ }K_{p}^{\prime}\subset K_{p}}\{[Y_{\Phi,K_{p}}({\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}})_{+}\backslash Y_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})\times K^{Y}_{\Phi,p}/K^{\prime,Y}_{\Phi,p}K^{Y,p}_{\Phi}]/K^{Y}_{\Phi,p}\}\\
&\cong\{\varprojlim_{K_{p}^{\prime}K^{p},\text{ }K_{p}^{\prime}\subset K_{p}}[Y_{\Phi,K_{p}}({\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}})_{+}\backslash Y_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})\times K^{Y}_{\Phi,p}/K^{\prime,Y}_{\Phi,p}K^{Y,p}_{\Phi}]\}/K^{Y}_{\Phi,p}\\
&\cong Y_{\Phi,K_{p}}({\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}})_{+}^{\overline{\ }}\backslash Y_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})\times K^{Y}_{\Phi,p}/K^{Y}_{\Phi,p}.\end{split}

The fourth line to the fifth line is by the Mittag-Leffler criterion.
So the lemma follows.
∎

The “
\mathscr{E}
-type” groups also decompose over
\mathscr{J}_{K_{p}}(Y_{\Phi})
.

By weak approximation for
\mathbb{G}_{m}
, the map defined by the quotient of
r_{E^{\prime}}(Y_{\Phi})
by
K_{\Phi,p}^{Y}
, namely,

(4.4)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces r_{E^{\prime},K_{p}}(Y_{\Phi}):\mathrm{Gal}(E^{\prime,ab}/E^{\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{0}(\mathbb{G}_{m}(\mathbb{A}_{E^{\prime}})/\mathbb{G}_{m}(E^{\prime}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\frac{\mathbb{A}_{E^{\prime}}^{\times}}{E^{\prime,\times}(E^{\prime}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{R})^{\times,+}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong\mathbb{A}_{E^{\prime},f}^{\times}/E^{\prime,\times,+}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{E^{\prime},f})/Y_{\Phi}(E^{\prime})_{+}^{\overline{\ }}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\frac{Y_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f})}{Y_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})_{+}^{\overline{\ }}K^{Y}_{\Phi,p}},}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{rec_{E^{\prime}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces[\mu_{\Phi,x}]}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N_{E^{\prime}/\mathbb{Q}}/K^{Y}_{\Phi,p}}

factors through

Y_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})/Y_{\Phi,K_{p}}({\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}})_{+}^{\overline{p}}\cong Y_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})\times K^{Y}_{\Phi,p}/Y_{\Phi,K_{p}}({\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}})_{+}^{\overline{\ }}K^{Y}_{\Phi,p}\subset\frac{Y_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f})}{Y_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})_{+}^{\overline{\ }}K^{Y}_{\Phi,p}}.

We abusively denote the resulting map
\mathrm{Gal}(E^{\prime,ab}/E^{\prime})\to Y_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})/Y_{\Phi,K_{p}}({\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}})_{+}^{\overline{p}}
by
r_{E^{\prime},K_{p}}(Y_{\Phi})
. Let
\widetilde{r}_{E^{\prime},K_{p}}(Y_{\Phi})
be the composition of
r_{E^{\prime},K_{p}}(Y_{\Phi})
with the natural map
\mathrm{Gal}(\overline{E}^{\prime}/E^{\prime})\to\mathrm{Gal}(E^{\prime,ab}/E^{\prime})
.
Let
\mathscr{E}_{K_{p}}(Y_{\Phi})
be the pullback of the extension

0\longrightarrow\mathscr{A}^{\circ}_{K_{p}}(Y_{\Phi})\longrightarrow\mathscr{A}_{K_{p}}(Y_{\Phi})\longrightarrow Y_{\Phi,K_{p}}({\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}})_{+}^{\overline{p}}\backslash Y_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})\longrightarrow 0

along
-\widetilde{r}_{E^{\prime},K_{p}}(Y_{\Phi})
.

Lemma 4.8
.

With the previous definitions, for any
j\in\mathscr{J}_{K_{p}}(Y_{\Phi})
, there is a natural map
\mathscr{E}_{{}^{j}{K_{p}}}(Y_{\Phi})\to\mathscr{E}(Y_{\Phi})
that fits the following diagram:

(4.5)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{A}^{\circ}_{{}^{j}{K_{p}}}(Y_{\Phi})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{E}_{{}^{j}{K_{p}}}(Y_{\Phi})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Gal}(\overline{E}^{\prime}/E^{\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{A}^{\circ}(Y_{\Phi})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{E}(Y_{\Phi})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Gal}(\overline{E}^{\prime}/E^{\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0.}

Moreover, the group
\mathscr{E}_{{}^{j}{K_{p}}}(Y_{\Phi})
is the pullback of the extension

0\longrightarrow\mathscr{A}^{\circ}_{{}^{j}{K_{p}}}(Y_{\Phi})\longrightarrow{\mathscr{A}_{{}^{j}{K_{p}}}(Y_{\Phi})}\longrightarrow Y_{\Phi,{}^{j}{K_{p}}}({\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}})_{+}^{\overline{p}}\backslash Y_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})\longrightarrow 0

along
-\widetilde{r}_{E^{\prime},{}^{j}{K_{p}}}(Y_{\Phi})
.

Proof.

This is self-explanatory from the constructions above.
∎

4.1.9.

Let us return to the settings for
(G_{0},X_{0})
and
(G_{2},X_{2})
.
Let
\Phi_{0}
be a cusp label representative of
(G_{0},X_{0})
and let
\Phi_{2}
be a cusp label representative of
(G_{2},X_{2})
.
Assume that there is an isogeny
P^{\prime}_{\Phi_{0}}\to Y^{\prime}_{\Phi_{2}}
whose kernel is in
Z_{G_{0}}\cap P^{\prime}_{\Phi_{0}}
.

We assume that
K_{\Phi_{0},p}\cap\mathscr{A}^{\circ}(P_{\Phi_{0}})
maps to
K_{\Phi_{2},p}^{Y}\cap\mathscr{A}^{\circ}(Y_{\Phi_{2}})
and that
g_{\Phi}K_{p}g_{\Phi}^{-1}\cap P^{\prime}_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
is the pullback of
g_{\Phi_{2}}K_{2,p}g_{\Phi_{2}}^{-1}\cap Y^{\prime}_{\Phi_{2}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
under the isogeny
P^{\prime}_{\Phi_{0}}\to Y^{\prime}_{\Phi_{2}}
.

Combining Lemma
4.7
, Lemma
4.8
and Proposition
4.6
, we obtain a
Y_{\Phi_{2}}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})\times\mathrm{Gal}(\overline{E}^{\prime}/E^{\prime})
-equivariant isomorphism (cf. (
4.1
))

(4.6)

\begin{split}&\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi_{2},p}^{Y},\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}\cong(\mathrm{Sh}^{+}_{P_{\Phi_{0}},\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}\times(\mathscr{A}(Y_{\Phi_{2}})/K_{\Phi_{2},p}^{Y}))/\mathscr{A}^{\circ}(P_{\Phi_{0}})\\
&\cong\coprod_{j\in\mathscr{J}_{K_{2,p}}(Y_{\Phi_{2}})}(\mathrm{Sh}^{+}_{{}^{j}{K_{\Phi_{0},p}},\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}\times(\widetilde{\mathscr{A}}_{{}^{j}{K_{2,p}}}(Y_{\Phi_{2}})/jK_{\Phi_{2},p}^{Y}j^{-1}))/{\widetilde{\mathscr{A}}^{\circ}_{{}^{j}{K_{2,p}}}(P_{\Phi_{0}})}\\
&\cong\coprod_{j\in\mathscr{J}_{K_{2,p}}(Y_{\Phi_{2}})}(\mathrm{Sh}^{+}_{{}^{j}{K_{\Phi_{0},p}},\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}\times\mathscr{A}_{{}^{j}{K_{2,p}}}(Y_{\Phi_{2}}))/\mathscr{A}^{\circ}_{{}^{j}{K_{2,p}}}(P_{\Phi_{0}}).\end{split}

Since every component in the disjoint union above descends to
E^{\prime}
, we can write
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi_{0},p}}
as a disjoint union over
E^{\prime}
,

\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi_{0},p}}\cong\coprod_{j\in\mathscr{J}_{K_{0,p}}(P_{\Phi_{0}})}{}^{j}{\mathrm{Sh}}_{K_{\Phi_{0},p}}.

Let
{}^{j}{\mathcal{S}}_{K_{\Phi_{0},p}K_{\Phi_{0}}^{p}}
be the normalization in
{}^{j}{\mathrm{Sh}}_{K_{\Phi_{0},p}K_{\Phi_{0}}^{p}}
of
\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{0},p}K_{\Phi_{0}}^{p}}
.

Define
{}^{j}{\mathcal{S}}_{K_{\Phi_{0},p}}:=\varprojlim_{K_{\Phi_{0}}^{p}\text{\ neat open compact}}{}^{j}{\mathcal{S}}_{K_{\Phi_{0},p}K_{\Phi_{0}}^{p}}
.

We also record a lemma.

Lemma 4.9
.

Let
K_{p}
be an open compact subgroup of
G(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
. Let
\Phi
be a cusp label representative of
(G,X)
. Then there is a finite Galois extension
F
of the reflex field
E:=E(G,X)
depending on
K_{p}
and
\Phi
, such that all connected components of
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi,p}}(G,X)_{\mathbb{C}}
are defined over
F\cdot E^{p}
, where
E^{p}
is the union of all finite extensions of
E
that are unramified at
p
.

Proof.

By
[
64
, Prop. 11.2 (c)]
, it suffices to show the lemma after replacing
(G,X)
with
(P_{\Phi,h},\hbar(D_{\Phi,h}))
. So we assume that
P_{\Phi}=G
is reductive without loss of generality.

Recall that by
[
20
, Thm. 2.6.3]
, the Galois group
\mathrm{Gal}(E^{ab}/E)
acts on the connected components by

(4.7)

\begin{split}\mathrm{Gal}(E^{ab}/E)\xrightarrow{rec_{E}}\pi_{0}(\mathbb{G}_{m}(\mathbb{A}_{E})/\mathbb{G}_{m}(E))\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathbb{A}^{\times}_{E}/E^{\times}(E\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{R})^{\times,+}\xrightarrow{[\mu_{x}]}\\
G(\mathbb{A}_{E,f})/G(E)^{\overline{\ }}_{+}\xrightarrow{N_{E/\mathbb{Q}}}G(\mathbb{A}_{f})/G(\mathbb{Q})^{\overline{\ }}_{+}K_{p}.\end{split}

This map sends open compact groups of
\mathbb{G}_{m}(E\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{Q}_{p})
to compact subgroups of
G(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
. So there is a possibly ramified finite field extension
F
of
E
, such that the image of
\mathrm{Gal}(E^{ab}/F)
under the map above is trivial at
p
. So we have the desired result.
∎

4.2.
Construction of strata and compactifications by quotients

We continue with the settings in §
1.4.4
. Let
(G,X_{a})
,
(G,X_{b})
and
K=K_{p}K^{p}\subset G(\mathbb{A}_{f})
be the Shimura data and the neat open compact subgroup chosen there. Since in most occasions only
(G,X_{b})
will appear in this subsection, we will omit the superscript
b
from many symbols in the first section.

Let
\Phi:=\Phi_{b}=(Q,X_{b}^{+},g^{b})
be any cusp label representative of
(G,X_{b})
. Set
P_{\Phi}:=P_{\Phi}^{b}
and
ZP_{\Phi}:=ZP_{\Phi}^{b}
. Let
K_{\Phi}:=K_{\Phi}^{b}=P_{\Phi}^{b}(\mathbb{A}_{f})\cap g^{b}Kg^{b,-1}
and let
\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}:=\widetilde{K}^{b}_{\Phi}=ZP_{\Phi}^{b}(\mathbb{A}_{f})\cap g^{b}Kg^{b,-1}
. Recall that
\mathrm{Sh}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}}:=\mathrm{Sh}_{\widetilde{K}^{b}_{\Phi}}(ZP^{b}_{\Phi},ZP^{b}_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})D_{\Phi_{b}})
is the mixed Shimura variety defined by
(ZP^{b}_{\Phi},ZP^{b}_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})D_{\Phi_{b}})
. In our conventions
\sigma
are cones in
\Sigma(\Phi)
,
\sigma_{0}^{\alpha}
are cones in
\Sigma_{0}^{\alpha}(\Phi_{0}^{\alpha})
, etc. Recall that we chose
\Sigma_{0}^{\alpha}
to be
induced
by
\Sigma
(see Proposition
1.47
). Let us explain how to use quotients to construct integral models
\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}}
,
\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi},\sigma}
and
\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}}(\sigma)
for
\mathrm{Sh}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}}
,
\mathrm{Sh}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi},\sigma}
and
\mathrm{Sh}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}}(\sigma)
, respectively.

We will study the following cases, where each of them is a special case of the one following it:

•

(Hyperspecial levels;
HS
) See
[
36
]
and
[
31
]
. Suppose that
G_{2}
is quasi-split and unramified at
p
. By
[
36
, Lem. 3.4.13]
, we can assume that
G_{0}
is chosen such that
G_{0,\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
is unramified. Then we can choose
G
such that the center of
G
is quasi-split and unramified at
p
(and
G^{\mathrm{der}}=G_{2}^{\mathrm{der}}
). Hence, we can and we shall choose
G_{0}
and
G
such that
G_{0}
and
G
are both quasi-split and unramified at
p
. Furthermore, we choose all of
g_{\alpha}
in
