G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
by
[
36
, Lem. 2.2.6]
.

Let
G_{2,{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}
be a smooth reductive model over
{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}
such that
G_{2,{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})=K_{2,p}
. As in the proof of
[
36
, Cor. 3.4.14]
, the center
Z^{\prime}
of
G
extends to a smooth reductive model
Z^{\prime}_{{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}
over
{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}
, and the kernel of the isogeny
G_{2}^{\mathrm{der}}\times Z^{\prime}\to G
also extends to a finite flat group over
{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}
. Hence, we choose a smooth reductive model
G_{{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}
of
G
over
{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}
such that
G_{2,{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}
embeds into
G_{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}
and
K_{p}=G_{{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
.
By
loc. cit
., we can choose a hyperspecial level
K_{0,p}^{\alpha}=G_{0,\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
, where
G_{0,\mathbb{Z}_{p}}
is a reductive group scheme over
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
, such that the kernel of
G_{0,\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\to G_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}
is finite and is contained in the center of
G^{\mathrm{der}}_{0,\mathbb{Z}_{p}}
. Here the subgroups
K_{0,p}^{\alpha}
are chosen to be the same. Write this group as
K_{0,p}:=K_{0,p}^{\alpha}
. Then we can choose
K^{{\ddagger},\alpha}_{p}=:K^{\ddagger}_{p}
to be the same. We also require the embeddings to be
p
-integral
as in
[
50
, Sec. 4.3]
.

•

(Bruhat-Tits stabilizer levels;
STB
) Fix a Bruhat-Tits stabilizer group scheme
\mathscr{G}_{x_{2}}
corresponding to a point
x_{2}
in the extended Bruhat-Tits building
\mathcal{B}(G_{2,\mathbb{Q}_{p}},\mathbb{Q}_{p})
. Choose a Hodge-type Shimura datum
(G_{0},X_{0})
such that there is a central isogeny
G_{0}^{\mathrm{der}}\to G_{2}^{\mathrm{der}}
which induces an isomorphism
(G_{2}^{\mathrm{ad}},X_{2}^{\mathrm{ad}})\cong(G_{0}^{\mathrm{ad}},X_{0}^{\mathrm{ad}})
. We further require that
(G_{0},X_{0})
satisfies the condition (3) in
[
33
, Lem. 4.6.22]
. By making this choice, we have that, for any place
v_{2}|p
of
E_{2}
and any place
v^{\prime}|v_{2}
of
E^{\prime}
,
\mathcal{O}_{E_{2,v_{2}}}\cong\mathcal{O}_{E^{\prime}_{v^{\prime}}}
. Note that the argument in the proof of
loc. cit.
for only part (3) still works for general cases.
Let
x
be the image of
x_{2}
in
\mathcal{B}(G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}},\mathbb{Q}_{p})
and
x
determines a Bruhat-Tits stabilizer group scheme
\mathscr{G}_{x}
. By our construction and by the definition of Bruhat-Tits stabilizer group schemes, we see that
\mathscr{G}_{x}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\cap G_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})=\mathscr{G}_{x_{2}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
. Let
K_{2,p}:=\mathscr{G}_{x_{2}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
and let
K_{p}:=\mathscr{G}_{x}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
.

For any
g_{\alpha}
, let
K_{0,p}^{\alpha}
be be the preimage of
g_{\alpha}K_{p}g_{\alpha}^{-1}
under
\pi^{b}
; since the kernel of
G_{0}\to G
is finite and is contained in the center of
G_{0}^{\mathrm{der}}
,
K_{0,p}^{\alpha}
is a Bruhat-Tits stabilizer subgroup of
G_{0}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
. Write
K_{0,p}^{\alpha}:=\mathscr{G}_{0}^{\alpha}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
, where
\mathscr{G}_{0}^{\alpha}
is a Bruhat-Tits stabilizer group scheme corresponding to a point
x_{0}^{\alpha}
in the extended building
\mathcal{B}(G_{0,\mathbb{Q}_{p}},\mathbb{Q}_{p})
. By
[
48
]
and as explained in
[
63
, 4.5.2, p. 86]
, for any Hodge embedding
(G_{0},X_{0})\hookrightarrow(\mathrm{GSp}(V,\psi),X^{\ddagger})
, up to replacing
V
with
V^{\oplus n}
for some
n
, we can choose a
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-lattice
V_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}^{\alpha}
such that
K_{0,p}^{\alpha}
is the stabilizer in
G_{0}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
of
V_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}^{\alpha}
.
By Zarhin’s trick, we can further replace
V
with
V^{\oplus 8}
, so that there is a self-dual
\mathbb{Z}
-lattice
V_{\mathbb{Z}}^{\alpha}
, such that
\mathrm{Stab}_{G_{0}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})}(V_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}^{\alpha})=K_{0,p}^{\alpha}
and
K_{p}^{{\ddagger},\alpha}:=\mathrm{Stab}_{G^{\ddagger}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})}(V_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}^{\alpha})
.
Now we have chosen for any
g_{\alpha}
a Hodge embedding
\iota^{\alpha}:(G_{0},X_{0})\hookrightarrow(\mathrm{GSp}((V,\psi)^{\perp r(\alpha)}),X^{{\ddagger},r(\alpha)})
for some positive integer
r(\alpha)
and a
\mathbb{Z}
-lattice
V_{\mathbb{Z}}^{\alpha}
satisfying the setup in
[
50
, Sec. 3.1]
and §
2.1
.
We can further assume that
r(\alpha)=r(\alpha^{\prime})
for different
\alpha\neq\alpha^{\prime}\in I_{G/G_{0}}
by further replacing all
r(\alpha)
with their lcm. Hence, we can and we will write
(\mathrm{GSp}((V,\psi)^{\perp r(\alpha)}),X^{{\ddagger},\alpha})
as
(G^{\ddagger},X^{\ddagger})
.

•

(Finite Intersections of Bruhat-Tits stabilizers;
\mathbf{STB}_{n}
) Slightly generalizing the case above, we still assume that
K_{p}\cap G_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})=K_{2,p}
but only require that
K_{p}:=\cap_{i=1}^{n}\mathscr{G}_{x_{i}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
, where
\{x_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}
is a set of
n
points in
\mathcal{B}(G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}},\mathbb{Q}_{p})
and
\mathscr{G}_{x_{i}}
are corresponding Bruhat-Tits stabilizer group schemes of
x_{i}
.
Pulling back to
G_{0}
, we have an intersection
K_{0,p}^{\alpha}:=\pi^{b,-1}(g_{\alpha}\cap_{i=1}^{n}\mathscr{G}_{x_{i}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})g_{\alpha}^{-1})=\cap_{i=1}^{n}\pi^{b,-1}(g_{\alpha}\mathscr{G}_{x_{i}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})g_{\alpha}^{-1})
. Each

in the (reduced) Bruhat-Tits Building of

over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
.
Write

.
Exactly as above, by
[

, 4.5.2]
and by Zarhin’s trick, we find a Hodge embedding

.
Let

By definition, (STB)

4.2.1.

We will omit and the readers can ignore the superscript
\alpha
before introducing Lemma

. Recall that
\pi:=\pi^{b}:(G_{0},X_{0})\to(G,X_{b})
is a map such that the kernel of

is finite and is in the center of
G_{0}^{\mathrm{der}}
. Let
\Phi_{0}:=(Q_{0},X_{0}^{+},g_{0})
be a cusp label representative in
\mathcal{CLR}(G_{0},X_{0})
mapping to
\Phi^{\ddagger}:=(Q^{\ddagger},X^{{\ddagger},+},g^{\ddagger})\in\mathcal{CLR}(G^{\ddagger},X^{\ddagger})

Recall that for
\gamma\in G^{\mathrm{ad}}_{0}(\mathbb{Q})
, there is a natural morphism
\mathrm{int}(\gamma):(G_{0},X_{0})\to(G_{0},\gamma\cdot X_{0})
, which induces isomorphisms (see

, 4.16]
)

\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi_{0}}}\to\mathrm{Sh}_{{}^{\gamma}{K_{\Phi_{0}}}},

which maps
[(x,p)]_{P_{\Phi_{0}}(\mathbb{A}_{f})\cap g_{0}Kg_{0}^{-1}}
to
[(\gamma(x),\gamma p\gamma^{-1})]_{{}^{\gamma}{(P_{\Phi_{0}}(\mathbb{A}_{f})\cap g_{0}Kg_{0}^{-1})}}
over complex points. Similarly, we have morphisms induced by
\gamma
for

and

By

, and we have

. Note that if

is quasi-parahoric (see Notation and Conventions).

Note that
\gamma
maps

to

(see §

). If

is a Bruhat-Tits stabilizer subgroup, so is

.

As in (STB), we can choose a Hodge embedding

and a Bruhat-Tits stabilizer subgroup

of

Lemma 4.10
.

Let
v
be any place of
E_{0}
over
p
.
Under the assumptions in (HS), (STB) or (DL) of (HS)/(STB), the action of
\gamma\in G_{0}^{\mathrm{ad}}(\mathbb{Q})
,
\gamma:\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi_{0}^{\alpha}}}\to\mathrm{Sh}_{{}^{\gamma}{K_{\Phi_{0}^{\alpha}}}}
, extends to an isomorphism
\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{0}^{\alpha}}}\to\mathcal{S}_{{}^{\gamma}{K_{\Phi_{0}^{\alpha}}}}
over
\mathcal{O}_{E_{0},(v)}
for any cusp label representative
\Phi_{0}^{\alpha}
and neat open compact subgroup
K_{0}^{\alpha}=K_{0,p}^{\alpha}K_{0}^{\alpha,p}
.
Moreover, the morphism
\gamma:\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{0}^{\alpha}}}\to\mathcal{S}_{{}^{\gamma}{K_{\Phi_{0}^{\alpha}}}}
as a morphism between torus torsors is equivariant under the action of
\mathbf{E}_{K_{\Phi_{0}^{\alpha}}}\to\mathbf{E}_{{}^{\gamma}{K_{\Phi_{0}^{\alpha}}}}
. Similarly, there are morphisms
\gamma:\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{K_{\Phi_{0}^{\alpha}}}\to\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{{}^{\gamma}{K_{\Phi_{0}^{\alpha}}}}
and
\gamma:\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{0}^{\alpha}},h}\to\mathcal{S}_{{}^{\gamma}{K_{\Phi_{0}^{\alpha}},h}}
extending the conjugation of
\gamma
over the generic fiber.

The integral models we constructed depend only on the choice of a Bruhat-Tits stabilizer subgroup in
G_{0}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
and are independent of the choice of the Hodge embedding in the construction made above.

Proof.

Write
\Phi_{0}:=\Phi_{0}^{\alpha}
and
K_{0}:=K_{0}^{\alpha}
to simplify the notation.
We only have to show the first two cases since the last case follows from taking relative normalizations. In Case (HS), it follows from
[
50
, Prop. 4.3.5]
that the action of
\gamma
extends to
\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{0}},p}\to\mathcal{S}_{{}^{\gamma}{K_{\Phi_{0}},p}}
. With the same reference, we see that this extended map is
P_{\Phi_{0}}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
-equivariant, and this proves the desired statement.

Since the schemes involved are normal and separated, and by gluing two morphisms over
\mathcal{O}_{E_{0},v}
and
E_{0}
that are identified over
E_{0,v}
, it suffices to show the statement over
\mathcal{O}_{E_{0},v}
. In Case (STB), by
[
50
, Thm. 4.1.5(1)]
, the tower
\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{0}}}\to\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{K_{\Phi_{0}}}\to\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{0}},h}
is a torus torsor
\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{0}}}\to\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{K_{\Phi_{0}}}
under
\mathbf{E}_{K_{\Phi_{0}}}
composing with an abelian scheme torsor
\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{K_{\Phi_{0}}}
over
\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{0}},h}
under
\mathcal{A}_{K_{\Phi_{0}}}
.

From the theory of canonical integral models of Shimura varieties of Hodge type (see
[
63
, Cor. 4.3.2]
,
[
14
, Thm. 4.14]
and
[
13
, Cor. 4.1.10]
) and Corollary
1.9
, there is a morphism
\gamma:\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{0}},h}\to\mathcal{S}_{{}^{\gamma}{K_{\Phi_{0}},h}}
extending the one over the generic fiber.
By the valuative criterion of properness and Weil’s extension theorem (see
[
9
, Sec. 4.4, Thm. 1]
), there is an extension
\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{K_{\Phi_{0}}}\to\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{{}^{\gamma}{K_{\Phi_{0}}}}
. For the extension of the whole
\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{0}}}
, we adopt the following method (with which we can also obtain the extension
\gamma:\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{K_{\Phi_{0}}}\to\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{{}^{\gamma}{K_{\Phi_{0}}}}
).

Suppose that
\mathcal{S}_{K_{0}}
and
\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{0}}}
are constructed from a Hodge embedding
\iota:(G_{0},X_{0},K_{0,p})\hookrightarrow(\mathrm{GSp}(V,\psi),X^{\ddagger},K^{\ddagger}_{p})
, and
\mathcal{S}_{{}^{\gamma}{K_{0}}}
and
\mathcal{S}_{{}^{\gamma}{K_{\Phi_{0}}}}
are constructed from another Hodge embedding
\iota^{\prime}:(G_{0},\gamma\cdot X_{0},{}^{\gamma}{K_{0,p}})\hookrightarrow(\mathrm{GSp}(V^{\prime},\psi^{\prime}),X^{{\ddagger},\prime},K_{p}^{{\ddagger},\prime})
. Let
V^{\prime\prime}:=V\oplus V^{\prime}
with symplectic pairing
\psi^{\prime\prime}
on it defined by the direct sum of the two factors. Let
\iota^{\prime\prime}:=\iota\times(\iota^{\prime}\circ\mathrm{int}(\gamma))
. Suppose that
K_{p}^{{\ddagger}}
(resp.
K_{p}^{{\ddagger},\prime}
) is the stabilizer in
\mathrm{GSp}(V,\psi)(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
(resp.
\mathrm{GSp}(V^{\prime},\psi^{\prime})(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
) of
\Lambda
(resp.
\Lambda^{\prime}
), where
\Lambda
(resp.
\Lambda^{\prime}
) is the base change to
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
of some self-dual
\mathbb{Z}
-lattice
V_{\mathbb{Z}}
(resp.
V_{\mathbb{Z}}^{\prime}
). Set
\Lambda^{\prime\prime}:=\Lambda\oplus\Lambda^{\prime}
,
V^{\prime\prime}_{\mathbb{Z}}:=V_{\mathbb{Z}}\oplus V_{\mathbb{Z}}^{\prime}
and
K_{p}^{{\ddagger},\prime\prime}:=\mathrm{Stab}_{\mathrm{GSp}(V^{\prime\prime},\psi^{\prime\prime})(\mathbb{Q}_{p})}(\Lambda^{\prime\prime})
. Let
G^{\ddagger}_{{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}
(resp.
G^{{\ddagger},\prime}_{{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}
and resp.
G^{{\ddagger},\prime\prime}_{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}
) be the smooth reductive group scheme over
{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}
whose group of
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-points is
K_{p}^{\ddagger}
(resp.
K^{{\ddagger},\prime}_{p}
and resp.
K^{{\ddagger},\prime\prime}_{p}
).
Then there are integral models
\mathcal{S}_{K_{0}}^{\prime\prime}
and
\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{0}}}^{\prime\prime}
of
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{0}}
and
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi_{0}}}
, respectively, constructed from
\iota^{\prime\prime}
and
\Lambda^{\prime\prime}
as in
[
63
]
,
[
13
]
and
[
50
]
by taking relative normalizations.

We claim that we have a diagram

(4.8)

\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{0}}}\longleftarrow\mathcal{S}^{\prime\prime}_{K_{\Phi_{0}}}\longrightarrow\mathcal{S}_{{}^{\gamma}{K_{\Phi_{0}}}}.

In fact, let
H^{\prime\prime}:=(\mathrm{GSp}(V,\psi)\times\mathrm{GSp}(V^{\prime},\psi^{\prime}))\times_{\mathbb{G}_{m}\times\mathbb{G}_{m}}\mathbb{G}_{m}
, where the left factor maps to
\mathbb{G}_{m}\times\mathbb{G}_{m}
by a product of similitude characters, and
\mathbb{G}_{m}
maps to
\mathbb{G}_{m}\times\mathbb{G}_{m}
by diagonal embedding. Then the embedding
G_{0}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{GSp}(V^{\prime\prime},\psi^{\prime\prime})
factors through
H^{\prime\prime}
, and this induces a Shimura datum
(H^{\prime\prime},X^{\prime\prime})
associated with
H^{\prime\prime}
. The morphism
(H^{\prime\prime},X^{\prime\prime})\hookrightarrow(\mathrm{GSp}(V^{\prime\prime},\psi^{\prime\prime}),X^{{\ddagger},\prime\prime})
is an embedding, and there are natural projections (see Remark
4.12
)

(\mathrm{GSp}(V,\psi),X^{\ddagger})\longleftarrow(H^{\prime\prime},X^{\prime\prime})\longrightarrow(\mathrm{GSp}(V^{\prime},\psi^{\prime}),X^{{\ddagger},\prime}).

Let
h
be any element in
X^{\prime\prime}
. Its weight cocharacter
\omega_{h}
sends
\mathbb{G}_{m}
to
\omega_{h}(\mathbb{G}_{m})
, and the intersection
C^{\prime\prime}
of
\omega_{h}(\mathbb{G}_{m})
and
\mathrm{Sp}(V,\psi)\times\mathrm{Sp}(V^{\prime},\psi^{\prime})
is isomorphic to
\mu_{2}
.
The split torus
\omega_{h}(\mathbb{G}_{m})
canonically extends to a split torus
\omega_{h}(\mathbb{G}_{m})_{{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}
over
{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}
, and the finite group
C^{\prime}
extends to a finite flat group scheme
C^{\prime}_{{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}
over
{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}
. Let
H^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}:=(G^{{\ddagger},\mathrm{der}}_{{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}\times G^{{\ddagger},\prime,\mathrm{der}}_{{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}\times\omega_{h}(\mathbb{G}_{m})_{{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}})/C^{\prime}_{{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}
be a reductive model over
{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}
extending
H^{\prime\prime}
. By
[
67
, Cor. 1.3]
and the proof of
[
31
, Lem. 4.7]
, there is an embedding

H^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}\hookrightarrow G^{{\ddagger},\prime\prime}_{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}

between reductive group schemes over
{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}
extending the embedding
H^{\prime\prime}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{GSp}(V^{\prime\prime},\psi^{\prime\prime})
. Let
K^{\prime\prime}_{p}=H^{\prime\prime}_{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
. Then
\iota_{H}^{\prime\prime}:(H^{\prime\prime},X^{\prime\prime},K_{p}^{\prime\prime})\hookrightarrow(\mathrm{GSp}(V^{\prime\prime},\psi^{\prime\prime}),X^{{\ddagger},\prime\prime},K_{p}^{{\ddagger},\prime\prime})
is
p
-integral in the sense of
[
50
, 4.3.2]
.
Suppose that
\Phi_{0}
maps to
\Phi^{\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{CLR}(H^{\prime\prime},X^{\prime\prime})
, and then to
\Phi^{{\ddagger},\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{CLR}(G^{{\ddagger},\prime\prime},X^{{\ddagger},\prime\prime})
.
Then by
[
50
, Prop. 4.3.5]
, the integral model
\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi^{\prime\prime},p}}:=\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi^{\prime\prime},p}}(P_{\Phi^{\prime\prime}},D_{\Phi^{\prime\prime}})
associated with any cusp label representative
\Phi^{\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{CLR}(H^{\prime\prime},X^{\prime\prime})
(constructed from taking relative normalization of
\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi^{{\ddagger},\prime\prime}}}
in
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi^{\prime\prime}}}
as in
[
50
, Sec. 4.1]
) has the extension property.
Then
\mathcal{S}^{\prime\prime}_{K_{\Phi_{0}}}
constructed from
\Phi^{{\ddagger},\prime\prime}
can also be constructed from taking relative normalization of
\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi^{\prime\prime}}}
in
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi_{0}}}
for suitable away-from-
p
levels. By the extension property and by taking quotient of away-from-
p
levels as in the first paragraph, we have a diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi_{0}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi_{0}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Sh}_{{}^{\gamma}{K_{\Phi_{0}}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi^{\ddagger}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi^{\prime\prime}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi^{{\ddagger},\prime}}}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{id}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma}

Now we have obtained the desired diagram by functoriality of relative normalizations.

By
[
50
, Thm. 4.1.5 (4)]
, the two morphisms in (
4.8
) are morphisms between
\mathbf{E}_{K_{\Phi_{0}}}\cong\mathbf{E}_{{}^{\gamma}{K_{\Phi_{0}}}}
-torsors over
\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{K_{\Phi_{0}}}\leftarrow\overline{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime\prime}_{K_{\Phi_{0}}}\to\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{{}^{\gamma}{K_{\Phi_{0}}}}
since this is so over generic fibers. Similarly,
\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{K_{\Phi_{0}}}^{\prime\prime}
(resp.
\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{{}^{\gamma}{K_{\Phi_{0}}}}
) is also an abelian scheme torsor under some
\mathcal{A}^{\prime\prime}
(resp.
{}^{\gamma}{\mathcal{A}}
) over
\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{0}},h}^{\prime\prime}
(resp.
\mathcal{S}_{{}^{\gamma}{K_{\Phi_{0}},h}}
) by
[
50
, Thm. 4.1.5(1)]
. More precisely, we have a commutative diagram of schemes

(4.9)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{0}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{0}}}^{\prime\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{S}_{{}^{\gamma}{K_{\Phi_{0}}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{K_{\Phi_{0}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{K_{\Phi_{0}}}^{\prime\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{{}^{\gamma}{K_{\Phi_{0}}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{0}},h}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{0}},h}^{\prime\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{S}_{{}^{\gamma}{K_{\Phi_{0}}},h}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}

By
[
21
, I. Prop. 2.7]
and characteristic zero theory, there is a diagram of isomorphisms between abelian schemes
\mathcal{A}_{K_{\Phi_{0}}}\xleftarrow{\sim}\mathcal{A}^{\prime\prime}\xrightarrow[\sim]{\gamma}{}^{\gamma}{\mathcal{A}}
; checking over an étale cover of the third row such that the abelian scheme torsors in the second row all split, the second row of (
4.9
) are isomorphisms between abelian scheme torsors. Similarly, the arrows in the first row of (
4.9
) are isomorphisms between torus torsors. Now we have obtained the desired extended isomorphism
\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{0}}}\cong\mathcal{S}^{\prime\prime}_{K_{\Phi_{0}}}\to\mathcal{S}_{{}^{\gamma}{K_{\Phi_{0}}}}
.

The last sentence follows from taking
\gamma=1
.
∎

The proof above essentially implies the following result:

Corollary 4.11
.

Assume that
(G_{2},X_{2})
is of Hodge type and choose
(G_{0},X_{0})=(G_{2},X_{2})
. In the second case (STB) or its (DL) case, choose any open compact subgroup
K_{0,p}
contained in a
fixed
Bruhat-Tits stabilizer subgroup
K_{0,p}^{*}
of
G_{0}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
and any
K_{0}^{p}
neat open compact in
G_{0}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
as before.
Fix
an admissible cone decomposition
\Sigma_{0}
of
(G_{0},X_{0},K_{0})
.

The good toroidal compactification
\mathcal{S}_{K_{0}}^{\Sigma_{0}}
of
\mathcal{S}_{K_{0}}
over
\mathcal{O}_{E_{0},(v)}
for the fixed cone decomposition
\Sigma_{0}
with associated integral models of boundary mixed Shimura varieties
\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{0}}}
satisfying
[
50
, Thm. 4.1.5]
and constructed from a Hodge embedding chosen as in
[
13
]
(and (STB)), if it exists, is independent of the choice of such embedding.

Proof.

The argument above works for any isomorphism between Shimura data
(G_{0},X_{0})\to(G_{0}^{\prime},X_{0}^{\prime})
. Let
\gamma=1
. Since the integral models with deeper level structures are constructed by taking relative normalizations from the integral models defined with the fixed Bruhat-Tits level structures, we assume
K_{0,p}=K_{0,p}^{*}
. We see that
\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{0}}}\to\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{K_{\Phi_{0}}}\to\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{0}},h}
is independent of the choice of the embedding as above. Combining this with
[
50
, Lem. A.3.4]
, we see the uniqueness of such toroidal compactifications.
∎

Remark 4.12
.

In
[
51
, Prop. 4.3]
, this result was also proved to study the change-of-parahoric morphisms of Hodge-type compactifications. We learned the construction of
(H^{\prime\prime},X^{\prime\prime})
from the second version of
[
35
, Prop. 4.3.2]
, but this part is now replaced by a new proof in the published version. The same argument above can extend the
full
functoriality result in
loc. cit.
to toroidal compactifications. We do not need to pursue this generality in this paper, but hope to record it in a future project with Shengkai Mao (see Remark
5.24
).

4.2.2.

Now we consider Case (
\mathrm{STB}_{n}
). Suppose
K_{0,p}=\cap_{i=1}^{n}K^{i}_{0,p}
, where
K^{i}_{0,p}
are Bruhat-Tits stabilizer subgroups of
G_{0}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
. As in the discussion under the third item (
\mathrm{STB}_{n}
),
we can choose a Hodge embedding
(G_{0},X_{0})\hookrightarrow(G^{{\ddagger},i,\prime},X^{{\ddagger},i,\prime})
where
G^{{\ddagger},i,\prime}=\mathrm{GSp}(V^{i},\psi^{i})
and a self-dual lattice
V_{\mathbb{Z}}^{i}
for each
i
, such that
K_{0,p}^{i}
is exactly the stabilizer of
V_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}^{i}
in
G_{0}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
. Let
K_{p}^{{\ddagger},i,\prime}
be the stabilizer in
G^{{\ddagger},i,\prime}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
of
V_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}^{i}
. Let
V_{\mathrm{sum}}:=\oplus_{i=1}^{n}V^{i}
and
V_{\mathrm{sum},\mathbb{Z}}:=\oplus_{i=1}^{n}V_{\mathbb{Z}}^{i}
. Let
K_{p}^{{\ddagger},\prime}
be the stabilizer of
V_{\mathrm{sum},\mathbb{Z}_{p}}
in
G^{{\ddagger},\prime}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
, where
G^{{\ddagger},\prime}:=\mathrm{GSp}(V_{\mathrm{sum}},\perp\psi^{i})
. Let
X^{{\ddagger},\prime}
be the union of Siegel upper and lower half-spaces induced by a
G^{{\ddagger},\prime}(\mathbb{R})
-conjugacy class of an element in
X_{0}
.
Define
H^{\prime}:=\prod_{i=1}^{n}G^{{\ddagger},i,\prime}\times_{\prod_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{G}_{m}}\mathbb{G}_{m}
, where the homomorphism from
\prod_{i=1}^{n}G^{{\ddagger},i,\prime}
to
\prod_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{G}_{m}
is given by the product of similitude characters of
G^{{\ddagger},i,\prime}
, and the homomorphism from
\mathbb{G}_{m}
to
\prod_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{G}_{m}
is given by the diagonal embedding. Note that the embedding
G_{0}\hookrightarrow\prod_{i=1}^{n}G^{{\ddagger},i,\prime}
factors through
H^{\prime}
: Indeed,
G_{0}
is generated by
G_{0}^{\mathrm{der}}
and
\omega(\mathbb{G}_{m})
(see Lemma
1.6
) and the restriction of the similitude character to
\omega(\mathbb{G}_{m})
is independent of the choice of Hodge embedding.
Let
X_{H^{\prime}}
be the
H^{\prime}(\mathbb{R})
-conjugacy class generated by an element in
X_{0}
and the embedding
G_{0}\to H^{\prime}
. The Shimura datum
(H^{\prime},X_{H^{\prime}})
is of Hodge type. Moreover, there is a diagram

(4.10)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(G_{0},X_{0})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(H^{\prime},X_{H^{\prime}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\prod_{i=1}^{n}G^{{\ddagger},i,\prime},\prod_{i=1}^{n}X^{{\ddagger},i,\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(G^{{\ddagger},\prime},X^{{\ddagger},\prime}).}

Let
G^{{\ddagger},i,\prime}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}
(resp.
G^{{\ddagger},\prime}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}
) be a smooth reductive model of
G^{{\ddagger},i,\prime}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
(resp.
G^{{\ddagger},\prime}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
) over
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
such that
G^{{\ddagger},i,\prime}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
(resp.
G^{{\ddagger},\prime}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
) is exactly the stabilizer of
V^{i}_{\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}
(resp.
V_{\mathrm{sum},\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}
).
By
[
10
, Prop. 1.7.6]
and
[
67
, Cor. 1.3]
(and the proof of
[
31
, Lem. 4.7]
), there are closed embeddings between reductive group schemes over
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
,
\prod_{i=1}^{n}G^{{\ddagger},i,\prime}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{GL}(V_{\mathrm{sum},\mathbb{Z}_{p}})
and
G^{{\ddagger},\prime}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{GL}(V_{\mathrm{sum},\mathbb{Z}_{p}})
extending
\prod_{i=1}^{n}G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}^{{\ddagger},i,\prime}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{GL}(V_{\mathrm{sum},\mathbb{Q}_{p}})
and
G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}^{{\ddagger},\prime}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{GL}(V_{\mathrm{sum},\mathbb{Q}_{p}})
, respectively; moreover, the flat base change from
{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}
to
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
of the schematic closure
G^{{\ddagger},i,\prime}_{{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}
(resp.
G^{{\ddagger},\prime}_{{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}
) of
G^{{\ddagger},i,\prime}
(resp.
G^{{\ddagger},\prime}
) in
\mathrm{GL}(V^{i}_{{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}})
(resp.
\mathrm{GL}(V_{\mathrm{sum},{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}})
) is isomorphic to
G_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}^{{\ddagger},i,\prime}
(resp.
G_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}^{{\ddagger},\prime}
) (cf.
[
36
, 2.3.1 and 2.3.2]
).
Let
h
be any element in
X_{H^{\prime}}
. Its weight cocharacter
\omega_{h}
sends
\mathbb{G}_{m}
to
\omega_{h}(\mathbb{G}_{m})
, and the intersection
C^{\prime}
of
\omega_{h}(\mathbb{G}_{m})
and
\prod_{i=1}^{n}G^{{\ddagger},i,\prime,\mathrm{der}}
is isomorphic to
\mu_{2}
.
The split torus
\omega_{h}(\mathbb{G}_{m})
canonically extends to a split torus
\omega_{h}(\mathbb{G}_{m})_{{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}
over
{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}
. The finite group
C^{\prime}
extends to a finite flat group scheme
C^{\prime}_{{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}
over
{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}
. So
H^{\prime}_{{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}:=\prod_{i=1}^{n}G^{{\ddagger},i,\prime,\mathrm{der}}_{{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}\times\omega_{h}(\mathbb{G}_{m})_{{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}/C^{\prime}_{{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}
is a reductive model over
{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}
extending
H^{\prime}
. By
[
67
, Cor. 1.3]
and the proof of
[
31
, Lem. 4.7]
again, there is a diagram of embeddings between reductive group schemes over
{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}
induced by (
4.10
)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{\prime}_{{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\prod_{i=1}^{n}G^{{\ddagger},i,\prime}_{{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G^{{\ddagger},\prime}_{{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{GL}(V_{\mathrm{sum},{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}).}

Let
\mathcal{H}_{p}:=H^{\prime}_{{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
. Then the embedding
(H^{\prime},X_{H^{\prime}},\mathcal{H}_{p})\hookrightarrow(G^{{\ddagger},\prime},X^{{\ddagger},\prime},K_{p}^{{\ddagger},\prime})
is
p
-integral (see
[
50
, 4.3.2]
).
Choose any cusp label representative
\Phi\in\mathcal{CLR}(H^{\prime},X_{H^{\prime}})
mapping to
\Phi^{{\ddagger},\prime}\in\mathcal{CLR}(G^{{\ddagger},\prime},X^{{\ddagger},\prime})
and to
\Phi^{i}\in\mathcal{CLR}(G^{{\ddagger},i,\prime},X^{{\ddagger},i,\prime})
. Choose a neat open compact subgroup
K^{{\ddagger},\prime,p}\subset G^{{\ddagger},\prime}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
(resp.
K^{{\ddagger},i,\prime}
) stabilizing
V_{\mathrm{sum},\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{p}}
(resp.
V_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{p}}^{i}
) and choose a neat open compact subgroup
\mathcal{H}^{p}\subset H^{\prime}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
contained in both
K^{{\ddagger},\prime,p}
and
\prod_{i=1}^{n}K^{{\ddagger},i,\prime}
. Define
\mathcal{H}:=\mathcal{H}_{p}\mathcal{H}^{p}
,
K^{{\ddagger},\prime}:=K^{{\ddagger},\prime}_{p}K^{{\ddagger},\prime,p}
and
K^{{\ddagger},i,\prime}:=K^{{\ddagger},i,\prime,p}K_{p}^{{\ddagger},i,\prime}
.
Let
\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathcal{H}_{\Phi}}:=\mathrm{Sh}_{P_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f})\cap g_{\Phi}\mathcal{H}g_{\Phi}^{-1}}(P_{\Phi},D_{\Phi})
, and let
\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi^{{\ddagger},\prime}}}
(resp.
\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi^{i}}}
) be the integral model corresponding to
\bm{\xi}_{(V_{\mathrm{sum},\mathbb{Z}},\perp\psi^{i}_{\mathbb{Z}}),\Phi^{\ddagger},K^{{\ddagger},\prime,p}}
(resp.
\bm{\xi}_{(V_{\mathbb{Z}}^{i},\psi^{i}_{\mathbb{Z}}),\Phi^{i},K^{{\ddagger},i,\prime,p}}
).
Let
\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{H}_{\Phi}}
be the normalization in
\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathcal{H}_{\Phi}}
of
\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi^{\ddagger}}}
. By
[
50
, Prop. 4.3.5]
, the inverse limit
\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{H}_{\Phi,p}}:=\varprojlim_{\mathcal{H}^{p}}\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{H}_{\Phi}}
has the extension property.
By Proposition
1.35
and
[
49
, Lem. 2.1.4]
,
\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{H}_{\Phi,p}}
is canonically isomorphic to the inverse limit
\varprojlim_{\prod_{i=1}^{n}K^{{\ddagger},i,\prime,p}}\prod_{i=1}^{n}\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi^{i}}}
. Moreover, let
\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{H}}
be the normalization of
\mathcal{S}_{K^{{\ddagger},\prime}}
in
\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathcal{H}}
. By
[
36
]
and
[
31
]
,
\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{H}_{p}}:=\varprojlim_{\mathcal{H}^{p}}\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{H}}
has the extension property.
Similarly, by Proposition
1.35
and
[
49
, Lem. 2.1.4]
,
\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{H}_{p}}
is an open and closed subscheme of
\varprojlim_{\prod^{n}_{i=1}K^{{\ddagger},i,\prime,p}}\prod_{i=1}^{n}\mathcal{S}_{K^{{\ddagger},i,\prime}}
. Hence,
\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{H}_{\Phi}}
is also the normalization in
\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathcal{H}_{\Phi}}
of
\prod_{i=1}^{n}\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi^{i}}}
, and
\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{H}}
is also the normalization in
\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathcal{H}}
of
\prod_{i=1}^{n}\mathcal{S}_{K^{{\ddagger},i,\prime}}
.

Hence, the discussion above implies the following lemma:

Lemma 4.13
.

With the conventions above, let
\Phi_{0}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{CLR}(G_{0},X_{0})
be a cusp label representative mapping to
\Phi
. Choose any open compact subgroup
K_{0,p}^{\prime}\subset G_{0}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
contained in
K^{{\ddagger},\prime}_{p}
(and therefore in
\mathcal{H}_{p}
) and any neat open compact subgroup
K_{0}^{\prime,p}\subset G_{0}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
. Choose neat open compact subgroups
K^{{\ddagger},\prime,p}
and
\prod_{i=1}^{n}K^{{\ddagger},i,\prime,p}
containing
K_{0}^{\prime,p}
. Let
K_{0}^{\prime}:=K_{0,p}^{\prime}K_{0}^{\prime,p}
.
Then the normalizations in
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{0}^{\prime}}(G_{0},X_{0})
of
\mathcal{S}_{K^{{\ddagger},\prime}}
and of
\prod_{i=1}^{n}\mathcal{S}_{K^{{\ddagger},i,\prime}}
are isomorphic, and the normalizations in
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi_{0}^{\prime}}}
(resp.
\overline{\mathrm{Sh}}_{K_{\Phi_{0}^{\prime}}}
and resp.
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi_{0}^{\prime}},h}
) of
\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi^{{\ddagger},\prime}}}
(resp.
\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{K_{\Phi^{{\ddagger},\prime}}}
and resp.
\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi^{{\ddagger},\prime}},h}
) and of
\prod_{i=1}^{n}\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi^{i}}}
(resp.
\prod_{i=1}^{n}\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{K_{\Phi^{i}}}
and resp.
\prod_{i=1}^{n}\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi^{i}},h}
) are isomorphic.

Proof.

Choose
\mathcal{H}^{p}
as above and containing
K_{0}^{\prime,p}
. By the discussion above, the normalizations in the statement are isomorphic to the normalization in
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{0}^{\prime}}
of
\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{H}}
and the normalization in
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi_{0}^{\prime}}}
(resp.
\overline{\mathrm{Sh}}_{K_{\Phi_{0}^{\prime}}}
and resp.
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi_{0}^{\prime}},h}
) of
\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{H}_{\Phi}}
(resp.
\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{\mathcal{H}_{\Phi}}
and resp.
\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{H}_{\Phi},h}
), respectively.
∎

4.2.3.

Now we come back to the situation that
\gamma
maps
\Phi_{0}
to
\gamma\cdot\Phi_{0}:=(\gamma Q_{0}\gamma^{-1},\gamma(X_{0}^{+}),\gamma g_{0}\gamma^{-1})
in Case (
\text{STB}_{n}
) and its (DL).

Suppose that
K_{0,p}
is contained in
K_{0,p}^{\prime}
, an intersection of
n
Bruhat-Tits stabilizer subgroups of
G_{0}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
. Then
{}^{\gamma}{K_{0,p}}\subset{}^{\gamma}{K_{0,p}^{\prime}}
.
We can now follow (
\text{STB}_{n}
) to choose a Hodge embedding
(G_{0},\gamma\cdot X_{0})\hookrightarrow(G^{{\ddagger},\gamma},X^{{\ddagger},\gamma})
as follows:
Suppose
K_{0,p}^{\prime}=\cap_{i=1}^{n}K^{i}_{0,p}
, where
K^{i}_{0,p}
are Bruhat-Tits stabilizer subgroups of
G_{0}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
. As in the discussion under the third item (
\mathrm{STB}_{n}
),
we can choose a Hodge embedding
(G_{0},\gamma\cdot X_{0})\hookrightarrow(G^{{\ddagger},i,\gamma},X^{{\ddagger},i,\gamma})
where
G^{{\ddagger},i,\gamma}=\mathrm{GSp}(V^{i},\psi^{i})
and a self-dual lattice
V_{\mathbb{Z}}^{i}
for each
i
, such that
{}^{\gamma}{K_{0,p}^{i}}
is exactly the stabilizer of
V_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}^{i}
in
G_{0}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
.
Let
V_{\mathrm{sum}}:=\oplus_{i=1}^{n}V^{i}
and
V_{\mathrm{sum},\mathbb{Z}}:=\oplus_{i=1}^{n}V_{\mathbb{Z}}^{i}
. Let
K_{p}^{{\ddagger},\gamma}
be the stabilizer of
V_{\mathrm{sum},\mathbb{Z}_{p}}
in
G^{{\ddagger},\gamma}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
, where
G^{{\ddagger},\gamma}:=\mathrm{GSp}(V_{\mathrm{sum}},\perp\psi^{i})
.

Suppose
\gamma\cdot\Phi_{0}
maps to a cusp label representative
\Phi^{{\ddagger},\gamma}\in\mathcal{CLR}(G^{{\ddagger},\gamma},X^{{\ddagger},\gamma})
. Let
K^{{\ddagger},\gamma}:=K_{p}^{{\ddagger},\gamma}K^{{\ddagger},\gamma,p}
for suitable neat open compact
K^{{\ddagger},\gamma,p}\subset G^{{\ddagger},\gamma}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
containing
{}^{\gamma}{K_{0}^{p}}
. Denote
K_{\Phi^{{\ddagger},\gamma}}=g_{\Phi^{{\ddagger},\gamma}}K^{{\ddagger},\gamma}g_{\Phi^{{\ddagger},\gamma}}^{-1}\cap P_{\Phi^{{\ddagger},\gamma}}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
.
We can still define an integral model
\mathcal{S}_{{}^{\gamma}{K_{\Phi_{0}}}}
(resp.
\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{{}^{\gamma}{K_{\Phi_{0}}}}
and resp.
\mathcal{S}_{{}^{\gamma}{K_{\Phi_{0}},h}}
) of
\mathrm{Sh}_{{}^{\gamma}{K_{\Phi_{0}}}}
(resp.
\overline{\mathrm{Sh}}_{{}^{\gamma}{K_{\Phi_{0}}}}
and resp.
\mathrm{Sh}_{{}^{\gamma}{K_{\Phi_{0}},h}}
) as the normalization of
\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi^{{\ddagger},\gamma}}}
(resp.
\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{K_{\Phi^{{\ddagger},\gamma}}}
and resp.
\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi^{{\ddagger},\gamma}},h}
) in
\mathrm{Sh}_{{}^{\gamma}{K_{\Phi_{0}}}}
(resp.
\overline{\mathrm{Sh}}_{{}^{\gamma}{K_{\Phi_{0}}}}
and resp.
\mathrm{Sh}_{{}^{\gamma}{K_{\Phi_{0}},h}}
).

Lemma 4.14
.

Under the assumptions in (
\text{STB}_{n}
) or its (DL), we have the same result as Lemma
4.10
. The integral models we constructed depend only on the choice of the collection of Bruhat-Tits stabilizers
\{K^{i}_{0,p}\}_{i=1}^{n}
and are independent of the choice of the collection of Hodge embeddings made above.

Proof.

Combine Lemma
4.10
with Lemma
4.13
.
∎

4.2.4.

Note that
\mathrm{Sh}_{K}(G,X_{b})(\mathbb{C})
is covered by
\coprod_{\alpha\in I_{G/G_{0}}}\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{0}^{\alpha}}(G_{0},X_{0})(\mathbb{C})
.
We can write
\mathrm{Sh}_{K}(G,X_{b})_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}
as

(4.11)

\begin{split}&\mathrm{Sh}^{+}(G_{0},X_{0})_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}\times(\mathscr{A}(G)/K)/\mathscr{A}^{\circ}(G_{0})\\
&\cong\coprod_{\alpha\in I_{G/G_{0}}}(\mathrm{Sh}^{+}(G_{0},X_{0})_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}\times(\mathscr{A}^{\circ}(G)\pi(G_{0}(\mathbb{A}_{f}))g_{\alpha}K/K))/\mathscr{A}^{\circ}(G_{0})\\
&\cong(\coprod_{\alpha\in I_{G/G_{0}}}\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{0}^{\alpha}}(G_{0},X_{0})_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}/\Delta(G_{0},G)\cdot g_{\alpha}K)/K\\
&\cong\coprod_{\alpha\in I_{G/G_{0}}}(\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{0}^{\alpha}}(G_{0},X_{0})_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}/\Delta(G_{0},G))/{}^{g_{\alpha}}{K}.\end{split}

In the equation above,
\Delta(G_{0},G):=\ker(\mathscr{A}(G_{0})\to\mathscr{A}(G))
.
Moreover, (
4.11
) is defined over
E_{0}
.

Now we construct the toroidal compactification
\mathcal{S}_{K}^{\Sigma}:=\mathcal{S}_{K}^{\Sigma}(G,X_{b})
as a disjoint union of quotients.

Denote
{}^{g_{\alpha}}{K}=g_{\alpha}Kg_{\alpha}^{-1}
as
{}^{\alpha}{K}
. Similarly, write
{}^{\alpha}{K^{p}}={}^{g_{\alpha}}{K^{p}}
and
{}^{\alpha}{K_{p}}={}^{g_{\alpha}}{K_{p}}
.
Denote
\Delta_{{}^{\alpha}{K}}(G_{0},G):=\ker(\mathscr{A}(G_{0})\to\mathscr{A}(G)/{}^{\alpha}{K})
.
Choose a neat open compact subgroup
K_{0}^{\alpha,p}
for each
\alpha
such that it is stabilized by
\Delta_{{}^{\alpha}{K}}(G_{0},G)
. This can be achieved because

Lemma 4.15
.

\Delta_{{}^{\alpha}{K}}(G_{0},G)
is compact.

Proof.

Without loss of generality, assume
\alpha
is trivial. For any open compact
K_{0}\subset G_{0}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
, write
\mathscr{A}(G_{0})=\coprod_{[g_{0}]\in\pi_{0}(\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{0},\mathbb{C}}(G_{0},X_{0}))}\mathscr{A}^{\circ}(G_{0})g_{0}K_{0}
. Also, write
\mathscr{A}(G)=\coprod_{[g]\in\pi_{0}(\mathrm{Sh}_{K,\mathbb{C}}(G,X_{b}))}\mathscr{A}^{\circ}(G)gK
. We now assume that
K_{0}
maps to
K
under
\pi
. We have decompositions

\mathscr{A}(G_{0})/K_{0}=\coprod_{[g_{0}]\in\pi_{0}(\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{0},\mathbb{C}}(G_{0},X_{0}))}\mathscr{A}^{\circ}(G_{0})/\mathscr{A}^{\circ}(G_{0})\cap{}^{g_{0}}{K_{0}}

and

\mathscr{A}(G)/K=\coprod_{[g]\in\pi_{0}(\mathrm{Sh}_{K,\mathbb{C}}(G,X_{b}))}\mathscr{A}^{\circ}(G)/\mathscr{A}^{\circ}(G)\cap{}^{g}{K}.

Both
\mathscr{A}^{\circ}(G_{0})\cap{}^{g_{0}}{K_{0}}
and
\mathscr{A}^{\circ}(G)\cap{}^{g}{K}
are arithmetic subgroups of
G^{\mathrm{ad}}(\mathbb{Q})^{+}
, and
\mathscr{A}^{\circ}(G_{0})/\mathscr{A}^{\circ}(G_{0})\cap{}^{g_{0}}{K_{0}}\cong G^{\mathrm{ad}}(\mathbb{Q})^{+}/\mathscr{A}^{\circ}(G_{0})\cap{}^{g_{0}}{K_{0}}
and
\mathscr{A}^{\circ}(G)/\mathscr{A}^{\circ}(G)\cap{}^{g}{K}\cong G^{\mathrm{ad}}(\mathbb{Q})^{+}/\mathscr{A}^{\circ}(G)\cap{}^{g}{K}
.
Then we see that the preimage of
[K]
under
\mathscr{A}(G_{0})/K_{0}\to\mathscr{A}(G)/K
is finite.
∎

Construction 4.16
.

Recall that we choose
\Sigma_{0}^{\alpha}
induced by
\Sigma
and we can choose them to be projective as in Proposition
1.47
.
Let

(4.12)

\mathcal{S}^{\Sigma}_{K}:=\coprod_{\alpha\in I_{G/G_{0}}}\mathcal{S}^{\Sigma_{0}^{\alpha}}_{K_{0}^{\alpha}}/\Delta_{{}^{\alpha}{K}}(G_{0},G).

We shall check that:

Lemma 4.17
.

In (
4.12
) above, the action of
\Delta_{{}^{\alpha}{K}}(G_{0},G)
on
\mathcal{S}^{\Sigma_{0}^{\alpha}}_{K_{0}^{\alpha}}
and the quotient of it are well defined. Hence, (
4.12
) defines a normal algebraic space that is proper over
\mathcal{O}^{\prime}_{(v)}
, and is representable by a normal scheme which is projective over
\mathcal{O}^{\prime}_{(v)}
if
\Sigma_{0}^{\alpha}
are induced by projective cone decompositions
\Sigma^{{\ddagger},\alpha}
for all
\alpha\in I_{G/G_{0}}
.

Proof.

By Lemma
4.10
, Lemma
4.14
and
[
50
, 4.1.3 and 4.1.12]
, there is an action of
\Delta_{{}^{\alpha}{K}}:=\Delta_{{}^{\alpha}{K}}(G_{0},G)
on the disjoint union of integral models
\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{0}^{\alpha}}}
for all
\Phi_{0}^{\alpha}\in\mathcal{CLR}(G_{0},X_{0})
. Since the cone decompositions
\Sigma_{0}^{\alpha}
are
induced
by
\Sigma
and since any element
d\in\Delta_{{}^{\alpha}{K}}
maps to
g_{\alpha}d^{\prime}g_{\alpha}^{-1}\in{}^{\alpha}{K}
, the action of
d^{\prime}
on
\Sigma
is trivial by admissibility of
\Sigma
, and therefore the action of
\Delta_{{}^{\alpha}{K}}
on the integral models
\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{0}^{\alpha}}}
induces an action of it on the disjoint union of strata
\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{0}^{\alpha}},\sigma_{0}^{\alpha}}\cong\mathcal{Z}_{\Upsilon_{0}^{\alpha},K_{0}^{\alpha}}
for all
\Upsilon_{0}^{\alpha}:=[(\Phi_{0}^{\alpha},\sigma_{0}^{\alpha})]\in\mathrm{Cusp}_{K_{0}^{\alpha}}(G_{0},X_{0},\Sigma_{0}^{\alpha})
.
Finally, by
[
50
, Lem. A.3.4]
, we have an action of
\Delta_{{}^{\alpha}{K}}
on
\mathcal{S}_{K_{0}^{\alpha}}^{\Sigma_{0}^{\alpha}}
that extends the actions on the individual strata.

Next, we shall explain why this quotient makes sense. By Lemma
4.15
above, the action of
\Delta_{{}^{\alpha}{K}}
on
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{0}^{\alpha}}
factors through a finite quotient, and therefore the same is true for
\mathcal{S}_{K_{0}^{\alpha}}^{\Sigma_{0}^{\alpha}}
since
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{0}^{\alpha}}
is dense in
\mathcal{S}_{K_{0}^{\alpha}}^{\Sigma_{0}^{\alpha}}
.

Consequently, the quotients in (
4.12
) exist as algebraic spaces by the Keel-Mori theorem
[
29
]
. (See also
[
12
, Thm. 3.1.13]
and
[
38
, Intro. p.17]
.)
The algebraic space
\mathcal{S}_{K}^{\Sigma}
is separated over the base by
[
12
, Thm. 3.1.12(i)]
(cf.
[
71
,
Lem. 05Z2
]
), and the morphism
\coprod_{\alpha\in I_{G/G_{0}}}\mathcal{S}^{\Sigma_{0}^{\alpha}}_{K_{0}^{\alpha}}\to\mathcal{S}_{K}^{\Sigma}
is finite by
[
12
, Thm. 3.1.13(i)(ii)]
and
[
71
,
Lem. 04NX
]
.
Combining it with
[
71
,
Lem. 08AJ
]
and
[
12
, Thm. 3.1.13(iii)]
, we have that the algebraic space
\mathcal{S}_{K}^{\Sigma}
is proper over
\mathcal{O}^{\prime}_{(v)}
. Finally,
\mathcal{S}_{K}^{\Sigma}
is normal as we can check normality over étale covers and combine
loc. cit.
again.

For the projectivity, note that
\mathcal{S}_{K_{0}^{\alpha}}^{\Sigma_{0}^{\alpha}}
will be projective if all
\Sigma_{0}^{\alpha}
’s for varying
\alpha
satisfy the condition in the second sentence of the statement (see
[
21
, Ch. V., Thm. 5.8]
and
[
44
, Thm. 7.3.3.4]
). Then
\mathcal{S}_{K}^{\Sigma}
is projective over the base since the quotient of a projective scheme by a finite group is projective (see
[
38
, Ch. 4, Prop. 1.5]
).
∎

The action of
\Delta_{{}^{\alpha}{K}}(G_{0},G)
is trivial on
\mathrm{Sh}_{K}^{\Sigma}
. So the finite morphism
\coprod_{\alpha\in I_{G/G_{0}}}\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{0}^{\alpha}}^{\Sigma_{0}^{\alpha}}\to\mathrm{Sh}_{K}^{\Sigma}
factors through the generic fiber of (
4.12
). By Zariski’s main theorem, the generic fiber of (
4.12
) is isomorphic to
\mathrm{Sh}_{K}^{\Sigma}
. Hence, (
4.12
) is indeed an integral model
of
\mathrm{Sh}_{K}^{\Sigma}
over
\mathcal{O}_{(v)}^{\prime}
.
\square

4.2.5.

Next, let us construct the strata for
\mathcal{S}_{K}^{\Sigma}
over
\mathcal{O}_{(v)}^{\prime}
.
Let
Q_{0}:=\pi^{-1}(Q)
and
X^{+}_{0}:=\pi^{-1}(X^{+}_{b})
. Up to an action of
Q_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})
, we can assume that
X^{+}_{0}
is nonempty.

Write
P_{0}:=P_{Q_{0}}
. There is a surjective map
P_{0}\to P_{\Phi}
.
We can write
G(\mathbb{Q})_{+}ZP_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f})g^{b}K
as a disjoint union

\coprod_{\alpha\in I_{G/G_{0}}}G(\mathbb{Q})_{+}\pi(G_{0}(\mathbb{A}_{f}))\alpha K\cap G(\mathbb{Q})_{+}ZP_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f})g^{b}K,

where
I_{G/G_{0}}\cong G(\mathbb{Q})_{+}\pi(G_{0}(\mathbb{A}_{f}))\backslash G(\mathbb{A}_{f})/K
.

Lemma 4.18
.

G(\mathbb{Q})_{+}ZP_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f})g^{b}K=G(\mathbb{Q})_{+}^{\overline{\ }}ZP_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f})g^{b}K=

\coprod_{\alpha\in I_{G/G_{0}}}\coprod_{\pi(g_{0}^{\alpha})\alpha\sim g^{b}}G(\mathbb{Q})_{+}\pi(P_{0}(\mathbb{A}_{f})g_{0}^{\alpha})g_{\alpha}K.

In the equation above, the second index runs over a complete set of representatives
\{\pi(g_{0}^{\alpha})\}
in
\pi(G_{0}(\mathbb{A}_{f}))
of
J_{G_{0}/P_{0}}^{\alpha}:=G(\mathbb{Q})_{+}^{\overline{\ }}\pi(P_{0}(\mathbb{A}_{f}))\backslash G(\mathbb{Q})_{+}^{\overline{\ }}\pi(G_{0}(\mathbb{A}_{f}))g_{\alpha}K/K
such that
\pi(g_{0}^{\alpha})g_{\alpha}
and
g^{b}
are equivalent in
G(\mathbb{Q})_{+}^{\overline{\ }}ZP_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f})\backslash G(\mathbb{A}_{f})/K
.

Proof.

The first equation follows from the fact that
G(\mathbb{Q})_{+}^{\overline{\ }}\mathcal{H}=G(\mathbb{Q})_{+}\mathcal{H}
for any open compact subgroup
\mathcal{H}
in
G(\mathbb{A}_{f})
.
Write
G_{0}(\mathbb{A}_{f})=\coprod_{g_{0}^{\alpha}}P_{0}(\mathbb{A}_{f})g_{0}^{\alpha}K_{0}^{\alpha}
. Then, for any
g_{0}^{\alpha}
,

G(\mathbb{Q})_{+}^{\overline{\ }}\pi(P_{0}(\mathbb{A}_{f})g_{0}^{\alpha}K_{0}^{\alpha})g_{\alpha}K=G(\mathbb{Q})_{+}^{\overline{\ }}\pi(P_{0}(\mathbb{A}_{f})g_{0}^{\alpha})g_{\alpha}K

is contained in
G(\mathbb{Q})_{+}^{\overline{\ }}ZP_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f})g^{b}K
if and only if
\pi(g_{0}^{\alpha})g_{\alpha}\sim g^{b}
.
∎

Here we add a “
\overline{\ }
” to
G(\mathbb{Q})_{+}ZP_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
since
G(\mathbb{Q})_{+}^{\overline{\ }}ZP_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
is a group but
G(\mathbb{Q})_{+}ZP_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
is not.

By Lemma
4.4
, Lemma
4.18
and
[
64
, Thm. 12.4]
, we have that

Proposition 4.19
.

With the notation as above, there are surjective maps

(4.13)

\coprod_{\alpha\in{I}_{G/G_{0}}}\coprod_{[\Phi_{0}^{\alpha}]=[(P_{0},X_{0}^{+},g_{0}^{\alpha})]\xrightarrow{\pi(\alpha)}[ZP^{b}(\Phi)]}\mathrm{Z}_{[\Phi_{0}^{\alpha}],K_{0}^{\alpha},\mathbb{C}}\longrightarrow\mathrm{Z}_{[ZP^{b}(\Phi)],K,\mathbb{C}},

(4.14)

\coprod_{\alpha\in{I}_{G/G_{0}}}\coprod_{[\Phi_{0}^{\alpha}]=[(P_{0},X_{0}^{+},g_{0}^{\alpha})]\xrightarrow{\pi(\alpha)}[ZP^{b}(\Phi)]}\Delta_{\Phi_{0}^{\alpha},K^{\alpha}_{0}}\backslash\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi_{0}^{\alpha}},h,\mathbb{C}}\longrightarrow\Delta^{ZP}_{\Phi,K}\backslash\mathrm{Sh}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi},h,\mathbb{C}},

(4.15)

\coprod_{\alpha\in{I}_{G/G_{0}}}\coprod_{[(\Phi_{0}^{\alpha},\sigma_{0}^{\alpha})]\xrightarrow{\pi(\alpha)}[ZP^{b}(\Phi,\sigma)]}\mathrm{Z}_{[(\Phi_{0}^{\alpha},\sigma_{0}^{\alpha})],K_{0}^{\alpha},\mathbb{C}}\longrightarrow\mathrm{Z}_{[ZP^{b}(\Phi,\sigma)],K,\mathbb{C}},

(4.16)

\coprod_{\alpha\in{I}_{G/G_{0}}}\coprod_{[(\Phi_{0}^{\alpha},\sigma_{0}^{\alpha})]\xrightarrow{\pi(\alpha)}[ZP^{b}(\Phi,\sigma)]}\Delta_{\Phi_{0}^{\alpha},K_{0}^{\alpha}}^{\circ}\backslash\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi_{0}^{\alpha}},\mathbb{C}}(\sigma_{0}^{\alpha})\longrightarrow\Delta_{\Phi,K}^{ZP,\circ}\backslash\mathrm{Sh}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi},\mathbb{C}}(\sigma),

and

(4.17)

\coprod_{\alpha\in I_{G/G_{0}}}\coprod_{[(\Phi_{0}^{\alpha},\sigma_{0}^{\alpha})]\xrightarrow{\pi(\alpha)}[ZP^{b}(\Phi,\sigma)]}\Delta_{\Phi_{0}^{\alpha},K_{0}^{\alpha}}^{\circ}\backslash\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi_{0}^{\alpha}},\sigma_{0}^{\alpha},\mathbb{C}}\longrightarrow\Delta_{\Phi,K}^{ZP,\circ}\backslash\mathrm{Sh}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi},\sigma,\mathbb{C}},

such that the images to right-hand sides of any two components in the disjoint unions of the left-hand sides of (
4.13
) (
4.14
) (
4.15
), (
4.16
) and (
4.17
) are disjoint or the same. All of the maps above algebraize and descend to the reflex field of
(G_{0},X_{0})
.

Construction 4.20
.

By Lemma
4.18
and Proposition
4.19
,
\mathrm{Z}_{[ZP^{b}({\Phi},\sigma)],K,\mathbb{C}}\cong\Delta^{ZP,\circ}_{\Phi,K}\backslash\mathrm{Sh}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi},\sigma,\mathbb{C}}
is isomorphic to the quotient

\coprod_{\alpha\in I_{G/G_{0}}}\coprod_{\pi(g_{0}^{\alpha})\alpha\sim g^{b}}(\Delta_{{}^{\alpha}{K}}(G_{0},G)(G_{0}(\mathbb{Q})_{+}\backslash G_{0}(\mathbb{Q})_{+}\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi_{0}^{\alpha}},\sigma_{0}^{\alpha},\mathbb{C}}))/\Delta_{{}^{\alpha}{K}}(G_{0},G),

where
\sigma_{0}^{\alpha}
is the unique cone in
\mathbf{P}_{\Phi_{0}^{\alpha}}
induced by the cone
\sigma
.
Hence, we define an
\mathcal{O}^{\prime}_{(v)}
-scheme associated with
[ZP^{b}(\Phi,\sigma)]
:

\mathcal{Z}_{[ZP^{b}(\Phi,\sigma)],K}:=\coprod_{\alpha\in I_{G/G_{0}}}\coprod_{\pi(g_{0}^{\alpha})\alpha\sim g^{b}}(\Delta_{{}^{\alpha}{K}}(G_{0},G)(G_{0}(\mathbb{Q})_{+}\backslash G_{0}(\mathbb{Q})_{+}\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{0}^{\alpha}},\sigma_{0}^{\alpha}}))/\Delta_{{}^{\alpha}{K}}(G_{0},G).

It is isomorphic to

\coprod_{\alpha\in I_{G/G_{0}}}\coprod_{\pi(g_{0}^{\alpha})\alpha\sim g^{b}}(\Delta_{{}^{\alpha}{K}}(G_{0},G)\Delta_{\Phi_{0}^{\alpha},K_{0}^{\alpha}}^{\circ}\backslash\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{0}^{\alpha}},\sigma_{0}^{\alpha}})/\Delta_{{}^{\alpha}{K}}(G_{0},G)

and to

\coprod_{\alpha\in I_{G/G_{0}}}\coprod_{\pi(g_{0}^{\alpha})\alpha\sim g^{b}}(\Delta_{{}^{\alpha}{K}}(G_{0},G)\mathcal{Z}_{[(\Phi_{0}^{\alpha},\sigma_{0}^{\alpha})],K_{0}^{\alpha}})/\Delta_{{}^{\alpha}{K}}(G_{0},G)

by
[
50
, Thm. 4.1.5(5)]
. When
K_{0}^{\alpha}
are neat,
\Delta^{\circ}_{\Phi_{0}^{\alpha},K_{0}^{\alpha}}
are trivial.
Since all the strata in the second disjoint union are locally closed in
\mathcal{S}_{K_{0}^{\alpha}}^{\Sigma_{0}^{\alpha}}
and since the actions of
\Delta_{{}^{\alpha}{K}}(G_{0},G)
on those strata and toroidal compactifications are equivariant, we see that the action of
\Delta_{{}^{\alpha}{K}}(G_{0},G)
on those strata factors through a finite quotient, and that we have a finite morphism
\mathcal{Z}_{[ZP^{b}(\Phi,\sigma)],K}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{S}^{\Sigma}_{K}
.
We shall check the following statement:

Lemma 4.21
.

The induced map
i:\mathcal{Z}_{[ZP^{b}(\Phi,\sigma)],K}\to\mathcal{S}_{K}^{\Sigma}
is a locally closed embedding. Thus we obtain a stratification on
\mathcal{S}_{K}^{\Sigma}
by all
ZP
-cusp labels with cones.

Proof.

Denote by
[\mathcal{Z}]
the image of
i
. (The image here is inductively defined, which means the schematic image defined in
\mathcal{S}_{K}^{\Sigma}
removing the images defined by other
\mathcal{Z}_{[ZP^{b}(\Phi,\sigma)],K}
’s with strictly smaller dimensions. In particular, it is automatically locally closed.) The map
i:\mathcal{Z}_{[ZP^{b}(\Phi,\sigma)],K}\to[\mathcal{Z}]
is finite, birational and a bijection over geometric points (see, e.g.,
[
28
, Cor. A.7.2.2]
).
To check this map is an embedding, we can check over the complete local ring at every geometric point.
At any geometric point
\bar{y}
of
\mathcal{Z}_{[(\Phi_{0}^{\alpha},\sigma_{0}^{\alpha})],K_{0}^{\alpha}}
, note that there is a structural morphism

{(\mathcal{S}_{K_{0}^{\alpha}}^{\Sigma_{0}^{\alpha}})}{}^{\wedge{\ }}_{\bar{y}}\cong{(\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{0}^{\alpha}}}(\sigma_{0}^{\alpha}))}{}^{\wedge{\ }}_{\bar{y}}\twoheadrightarrow{(\mathcal{Z}_{[(\Phi_{0}^{\alpha},\sigma_{0}^{\alpha})],K_{0}^{\alpha}})}{}^{\wedge{\ }}_{\bar{y}}

whose pre-composition with the natural inclusion
{(\mathcal{Z}_{[(\Phi_{0}^{\alpha},\sigma_{0}^{\alpha})],K_{0}^{\alpha}})}{}^{\wedge{\ }}_{\bar{y}}\hookrightarrow{(\mathcal{S}_{K_{0}^{\alpha}}^{\Sigma_{0}^{\alpha}})}{}^{\wedge{\ }}_{\bar{y}}
is the identity. The structural morphism is defined by the canonical projection of a twisted affine torus embedding with respect to a cone
\sigma_{0}^{\alpha}
to its
\sigma_{0}^{\alpha}
-stratum.

Let
\bar{x}
be any geometric point on
\mathcal{Z}_{[ZP^{b}(\Phi,\sigma)],K}
. Denote the image of
\bar{x}
on
[\mathcal{Z}]
by
[\bar{x}]
, and suppose
[\bar{x}]
can be lifted to a geometric point on
\mathcal{Z}_{[(\Phi_{0}^{\alpha},\sigma_{0}^{\alpha})],K_{0}^{\alpha}}
.
We then obtain a sequence

{(\mathcal{Z}_{[ZP^{b}(\Phi,\sigma)],K})}{}^{\wedge{\ }}_{\bar{x}}\to{(\mathcal{S}_{K}^{\Sigma})}{}^{\wedge{\ }}_{[\bar{x}]}\cong({(\mathcal{S}^{\Sigma_{0}^{\alpha}}_{K_{0}^{\alpha}})}{}^{\wedge{\ }}_{\pi^{b,-1}([\bar{x}])})^{\Delta_{{}^{\alpha}{K}}(G_{0},G)}\to{(\mathcal{Z}_{[ZP^{b}(\Phi,\sigma)],K})}{}^{\wedge{\ }}_{\bar{x}}

whose composition is the identity. This induces an isomorphism
{(\mathcal{Z}_{[ZP^{b}(\Phi,\sigma)],K})}{}^{\wedge{\ }}_{\bar{x}}\cong{([\mathcal{Z}])}{}^{\wedge{\ }}_{[\bar{x}]}
. The desired assertion that
\mathcal{Z}_{[ZP^{b}(\Phi,\sigma)],K}\cong[\mathcal{Z}]
is now proved.
∎

Since the quotient map from the disjoint union of
\mathcal{Z}_{[(\Phi_{0}^{\alpha},\sigma_{0}^{\alpha})],K_{0}^{\alpha}}
to
\mathcal{Z}_{[ZP^{b}(\Phi,\sigma)],K}
is finite, by induction on the dimension of
\mathcal{Z}_{[ZP^{b}(\Phi,\sigma)],K}
and by Lemma
4.21
, we have a stratification of
\mathcal{S}_{K}^{\Sigma}
by all
\mathcal{Z}_{[ZP^{b}(\Phi,\sigma)],K}
extending that defined by
ZP
-cusp labels with cones on the generic fiber, and we have that those strata are normal, are locally closed in
\mathcal{S}_{K}^{\Sigma}
, and are flat over
\mathcal{O}^{\prime}_{(v)}
.

We then define finer strata
\mathcal{Z}_{[(\Phi,\sigma)],K}
as relative normalizations of
\mathcal{Z}_{[ZP^{b}(\Phi,\sigma)],K}
in
\mathrm{Z}_{[(\Phi,\sigma)],K}
. Since the embedding of
\mathrm{Z}_{[(\Phi,\sigma)],K}
in
\mathrm{Z}_{[ZP^{b}(\Phi,\sigma)],K}
is open and closed,
\mathcal{Z}_{[(\Phi,\sigma)],K}
is open and closed in
\mathcal{Z}_{[ZP^{b}(\Phi,\sigma)],K}
. Hence,
\mathcal{Z}_{[(\Phi,\sigma)],K}
are locally closed in
\mathcal{S}_{K}^{\Sigma}
. Moreover, by construction, we have that

\mathcal{S}_{K}^{\Sigma}\cong\coprod_{[ZP^{b}(\Phi,\sigma)]\in\mathrm{Cusp}_{K}^{ZP}(G,X_{b},\Sigma)}\mathcal{Z}_{[ZP^{b}(\Phi,\sigma)],K}\cong\coprod_{\Upsilon=[(\Phi,\sigma)]\in\mathrm{Cusp}_{K}(G,X_{b},\Sigma)}\mathcal{Z}_{[(\Phi,\sigma)],K},

which gives us stratifications of
\mathcal{S}_{K}^{\Sigma}
given by
ZP
-cusp labels and cusp labels with cones, extending the stratifications of both types of cusp labels with cones over the generic fiber.
\square

4.2.6.

Now we are ready to find an integral model of
\mathrm{Sh}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}}:=\mathrm{Sh}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}^{b}}(ZP^{b}_{\Phi},ZP^{b}_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})D_{\Phi})
.
Set

I_{ZP_{\Phi}/P_{0},K}:=\mathrm{Stab}_{ZP_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})}(D_{Q_{0},X^{+}_{0}})\pi(P_{0}(\mathbb{A}_{f}))\backslash ZP_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f})g^{b}K/K.

Since
P_{0}(\mathbb{Q})
acts on connected components of
D_{Q_{0},X^{+}_{0}}
transitively, we have

I_{ZP_{\Phi}/P_{0},K}\cong\mathrm{Stab}_{ZP_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})}(D_{Q_{0},X_{0}^{+}}^{+})\pi(P_{0}(\mathbb{A}_{f}))\backslash ZP_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f})g^{b}K/K.

For any
\beta\in I_{\Phi}:=I_{ZP_{\Phi}/P_{0},K}
, we consider the image of
\beta
in

J_{\Phi}:=G(\mathbb{Q})_{+}^{\overline{\ }}\pi(P_{0}(\mathbb{A}_{f}))\backslash G(\mathbb{Q})_{+}^{\overline{\ }}ZP_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f})g^{b}K/K.

By Proposition
4.19
, for any
\beta
, we choose any representative
e_{\beta}\in ZP_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f})g^{b}K
for
\beta
, and there is a
\gamma_{\beta}\in G(\mathbb{Q})_{+}
and a cusp label representative
\Phi_{\beta}=(Q_{0}^{\gamma_{\beta}},X^{+}_{0},g_{\beta})
in
\mathcal{CLR}(G_{0},X_{0})
such that
\gamma_{\beta}g_{\beta}g_{\alpha}=e_{\beta}k
for some
k\in K
,

\mathrm{Sh}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}}\cong\coprod_{\beta\in I_{\Phi}}\gamma_{\beta}\cdot\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi_{\beta}}}/\Delta(P_{0},ZP_{\Phi}/ZP_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f})\cap{}^{\gamma_{\beta}g_{\beta}g_{\alpha}}{K}),

where
\Delta(P_{0},ZP_{\Phi}/ZP_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f})\cap{}^{\gamma_{\beta}g_{\beta}g_{\alpha}}{K}):=\ker(\mathscr{A}(P_{0})\to\mathscr{A}(ZP_{\Phi})/ZP_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f})\cap{}^{\gamma_{\beta}g_{\beta}g_{\alpha}}{K})
and
g_{\alpha}
is determined by the image of
\beta
in
I_{G/G_{0}}
. Set
K^{\beta}:=ZP_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f})\cap{}^{\gamma_{\beta}g_{\beta}g_{\alpha}}{K}
and
\Delta_{K^{\beta}}(P_{0},ZP_{\Phi}):=\Delta(P_{0},ZP_{\Phi}/ZP_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f})\cap{}^{\gamma_{\beta}g_{\beta}g_{\alpha}}{K})
.

Lemma 4.22
.

\Delta_{{K}^{\beta}}(P_{0},ZP_{\Phi})
is compact.

Proof.

For any open compact subgroup
K_{\Phi_{0}}\subset P_{0}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
mapping to
K^{\beta}
under
\pi
, we have decompositions

\mathscr{A}(P_{0})/K_{\Phi_{0}}=\coprod_{[p_{0}]\in P_{0}(\mathbb{Q})_{+}\backslash P_{0}(\mathbb{A}_{f})/K_{\Phi_{0}}}\mathscr{A}^{\circ}(P_{0})/\mathscr{A}^{\circ}(P_{0})\cap{}^{p_{0}}{K_{\Phi_{0}}}

and

\mathscr{A}(ZP_{\Phi})/K^{\beta}=\coprod_{[p]\in ZP_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})_{+}\backslash ZP_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f})/K^{\beta}}\mathscr{A}^{\circ}(ZP_{\Phi})/\mathscr{A}^{\circ}(ZP_{\Phi})\cap{}^{p}{(K^{\beta})}.

Both
\mathscr{A}^{\circ}(P_{0})\cap{}^{p_{0}}{K_{\Phi_{0}}}
and
\mathscr{A}^{\circ}(ZP_{\Phi})\cap{}^{p}{(K^{\beta})}
are arithmetic subgroups of
P_{0}^{\mathrm{ad}}(\mathbb{Q})^{+}
, and
\mathscr{A}^{\circ}(P_{0})/\mathscr{A}^{\circ}(P_{0})\cap{}^{p_{0}}{K_{\Phi_{0}}}\cong P_{0}^{\mathrm{ad}}(\mathbb{Q})^{+}/\mathscr{A}^{\circ}(P_{0})\cap{}^{p_{0}}{K_{\Phi_{0}}}
and
\mathscr{A}^{\circ}(ZP_{\Phi})/\mathscr{A}^{\circ}(ZP_{\Phi})\cap{}^{p}{(K^{\beta})}\cong P_{0}^{\mathrm{ad}}(\mathbb{Q})^{+}/\mathscr{A}^{\circ}(ZP_{\Phi})\cap{}^{p}{(K^{\beta})}
.
Then we see that the preimage of
[K^{\beta}]
under
\mathscr{A}(P_{0})/K_{\Phi_{0}}\to\mathscr{A}(G)/K^{\beta}
is finite.
∎

Hence, we can write
\mathrm{Sh}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}}
as

\coprod_{\beta\in I_{\Phi}}\gamma_{\beta}\cdot\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi_{\beta}}}/\Delta_{K^{\beta}}(P_{0},ZP_{\Phi}).

Construction 4.23
.

Set
\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}}:=\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}^{b}_{\Phi}}(ZP_{\Phi}^{b},ZP^{b}_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})D_{\Phi}):=

\coprod_{\beta\in I_{\Phi}}\gamma_{\beta}\cdot\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{\beta}}}/\Delta_{K^{\beta}}(P_{0},ZP_{\Phi})

The quotient makes sense since the action of
\Delta_{K^{\beta}}(P_{0},ZP_{\Phi})
makes sense by Lemma
4.10
, Lemma
4.14
, and
[
50
, 4.1.3]
, and factors through a finite group by Lemma
4.22
.

Similarly, we construct

\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}}:=\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}^{b}}(ZP^{b}_{\Phi},ZP^{b}_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})D_{\Phi_{b}}):=\coprod_{\beta\in I_{\Phi}}\gamma_{\beta}\cdot\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{K_{\Phi_{\beta}}}/\Delta_{K^{\beta}}(P_{0},ZP_{\Phi}),

and

\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi},h}:=\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}^{b},h}(ZP^{b}_{\Phi},ZP^{b}_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})D_{\Phi_{b}}):=\coprod_{\beta\in I_{\Phi}}\gamma_{\beta}\cdot\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{\beta}},h}/\Delta_{K^{\beta}}(P_{0},ZP_{\Phi}).

Consequently, there is a tower of
\mathcal{O}^{\prime}_{(v)}
-schemes

\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}}\longrightarrow\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}}\longrightarrow\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi},h}.

By the construction above and by
[
50
, 4.1.5(4)]
(cf. Theorem
2.46
(3)), the first morphism above is affine, while the second morphism is proper.
\square

Denote
\Phi_{\beta^{\prime}}:=\gamma_{\beta}\cdot\Phi_{\beta}
.

Now we shall check the following statement:

Proposition 4.24
.

In Cases (HS), (
\text{STB}_{n}
) and (DL),
\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}}
,
\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}}
and
\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi},h}
are independent of the choice of representatives
e_{\beta}
and the pairs
(\gamma_{\beta},g_{\beta})
for all
\beta
in the construction.

Moreover, for any
\gamma\in G(\mathbb{Q})
and
q^{\prime}\in ZP_{\Phi_{2}}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
such that
\Phi_{1}\sim_{ZP}\Phi_{2}
under the equivalence
\Phi_{1}\xrightarrow[\sim]{(\gamma,q^{\prime})_{K}}\Phi_{2}
, the isomorphism of mixed Shimura varieties induced by this equivalence extends to an isomorphism between integral models
(\gamma,q^{\prime}):\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi_{1}}}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi_{2}}}
.

The statement in the last paragraph also holds if we replace
\mathcal{S}_{?}
with
\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{?}
and
\mathcal{S}_{?,h}
. The isomorphisms induced by
(\gamma,q^{\prime})
are compatible with the towers
\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}}\to\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}}\to\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi},h}
.

Proof.

By Lemma
4.10
, Lemma
4.14
and
[
50
, 4.1.3]
, different choices of
(e_{\beta};\gamma_{\beta},g_{\beta})
for the same
\beta
will induce isomorphisms between the models
\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{\beta^{\prime}}}}
constructed as above. Now we show the second paragraph.

By Lemma
4.14
and the constructions above, the statements hold for
(\gamma,1)
since the morphism
\gamma:\mathcal{S}_{K_{\beta^{\prime}}}\to\mathcal{S}_{{}^{\gamma}{K_{\beta^{\prime}}}}
is well defined. We only need to show the case where
\gamma=1
and
q^{\prime}
is non-trivial.
In fact, we can directly define a morphism of
q^{\prime}
; if we can do so, then the statement follows from separatedness and normality of the schemes involved.
Suppose
\Phi\xrightarrow[\sim]{(1,q^{\prime})_{K}}\Phi_{1}
.
Fix a complete set of representatives
\{e_{\beta}\}
for
I_{ZP_{\Phi}/P_{0}}
. For any
q^{\prime}\in ZP_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
,
\{e_{\beta}^{q^{\prime}}:=e_{\beta}q^{\prime}\}
is a complete set of representatives for
\mathrm{Stab}_{ZP_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})}(D_{Q_{0},X_{0}^{+}})\pi(P_{0}(\mathbb{A}_{f}))\backslash ZP_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f})(q^{\prime})^{-1}g^{b}K/K
. We still assign
(\gamma_{\beta},g_{\beta})
to
e_{\beta}^{q^{\prime}}
and construct
\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi_{1}}}
as above. By the last paragraph, the construction of
\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi_{1}}}
is independent of the choice of such assignment.
Then there is an isomorphism
q^{\prime}:\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi_{1}}}
induced by the map
e_{\beta}\mapsto e_{\beta}^{q^{\prime}}
extending the isomorphism over the generic fiber sending the scheme
\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{\beta^{\prime}}}}
constructed for the index
e_{\beta}
identically to the scheme constructed for the index
e_{\beta}^{q^{\prime}}
.
∎

4.2.7.

Let us study the integral models we just constructed.

For any admissible cone decomposition
\Sigma_{0}
of
(G_{0},X_{0},K_{0}^{\alpha})
and
\Phi_{\beta^{\prime}}
, define
\Delta_{K^{\beta}}^{\circ}(P_{0},G)
to be
the group consisting of the elements
(h,\gamma^{-1})\in\Delta_{K^{\beta}}(P_{0},G):=\ker(\mathscr{A}(P_{0})\times^{P_{0}^{\mathrm{ad}}(\mathbb{Q})^{+}}Q_{0}^{\mathrm{ad}}(\mathbb{Q})^{+}\to\mathscr{A}(G)/{}^{\gamma_{\beta}g_{\beta}g_{\alpha}}{K})
such that
\gamma
stabilizes
\Sigma(\Phi_{\beta^{\prime}})
, where we denote
Q_{0}^{\mathrm{ad}}(\mathbb{Q})^{+}:=Q^{\mathrm{ad}}_{0}(\mathbb{Q})\cap G^{\mathrm{ad}}_{0}(\mathbb{Q})^{+}
.

In fact,
\Delta_{\beta}:=\Delta_{K^{\beta}}(P_{0},G)
is a generalization of the group
\Delta_{\Phi,K}
defined by Pink (see
[
64
, 6.3 and 7.3]
). To explain this similarity, we convert the right action of
(h,\gamma^{-1})\in\Delta_{\beta}
to
[\gamma h\gamma^{-1}]\circ[\gamma]
, where
[\gamma]
is the left action of
\gamma
and
[\gamma h\gamma^{-1}]
is the right action of
\gamma h\gamma^{-1}
.
Let

\begin{split}&\widetilde{\Delta}_{\beta}:=\{(h_{1},\gamma_{1})\in P_{0}(\mathbb{A}_{f})\times Q^{\mathrm{ad}}_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})^{+}|\gamma_{1}\text{ is lifted to an element }\gamma^{*}_{1}\in Q_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})_{+},\\
&\text{ and }\gamma_{1}^{*}=h_{1}\text{ modulo }{}^{\gamma_{\beta}g_{\beta}g_{\alpha}}{K}\text{ on the right in }G(\mathbb{A}_{f})\},\end{split}

where
Q_{\Phi}^{\mathrm{ad}}(\mathbb{Q})^{+}:=Q_{\Phi}^{\mathrm{ad}}(\mathbb{Q})\cap G^{\mathrm{ad}}(\mathbb{Q})^{+}=Q_{0}^{\mathrm{ad}}(\mathbb{Q})^{+}
.
We have that
(h,\gamma^{-1})\in\Delta_{\beta}
if and only if there is a lifting
\gamma_{1}^{*}
of
\gamma
to
Q_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})_{+}
such that
(h,\gamma^{-1})(\gamma_{1}^{*,-1},\gamma)=(h\gamma^{-1}\gamma_{1}^{*,-1}\gamma,1)=(h\gamma_{1}^{*,-1},1)=(k,1)
for some
k\in{}^{\gamma_{\beta}g_{\beta}g_{\alpha}}{K}
. We then have
(h,\gamma^{-1})\in\Delta_{\beta}
if and only if
\gamma_{1}^{*}=\gamma h\gamma^{-1}
modulo
{}^{\gamma_{\beta}g_{\beta}g_{\alpha}}{K}
. As a result, there is a surjective homomorphism

\widetilde{\Delta}_{\beta}\longrightarrow\Delta_{\beta}

with the assignment
(\gamma h\gamma^{-1},\gamma)\mapsto(h,\gamma^{-1})
, which converts the right action of
\Delta_{\beta}
to
[\gamma h\gamma^{-1}]\circ[\gamma]
. Note that the action of
\widetilde{\Delta}_{\beta}
modulo
P_{0}(\mathbb{Q})
coincides with the action of Pink’s group
\Delta_{\Phi,K}
when
(G_{0},X_{0})=(G,X_{b})
,
P_{0}=P_{\Phi}
and
\gamma_{\beta}=g_{\alpha}=1
.

By
[
64
, Thm. 6.19(a)]
and
[
50
, 2.1.19]
, the definition of
\Delta_{K^{\beta}}^{\circ}(P_{0},G)
is independent of the choice of the cone decomposition
\Sigma_{0}
.

Lemma 4.25
.

Recall that we assume
K^{p}
is
neat
. With the conventions and assumptions above, and in all cases,

(1)

We have
\Delta^{\circ}_{K^{\beta}}(P_{0},G)=\Delta_{K^{\beta}}(P_{0},ZP_{\Phi})
.

(2)

The group
\Delta_{K^{\beta}}(P_{0},ZP_{\Phi})
acts equivariantly on the tower of schemes
\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{\beta^{\prime}}}}\to\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{K_{\Phi_{\beta^{\prime}}}}\to\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{\beta^{\prime}}},h}
; the quotient induced by this action on each scheme in the tower is finite étale. More precisely, the action of
\Delta_{K^{\beta}}(P_{0},ZP_{\Phi})
on
\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{\beta^{\prime}}}}
(resp.
\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{K_{\Phi_{\beta^{\prime}}}}
and resp.
\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{\beta^{\prime}}},h}
) factors through some finite group
H_{\beta}
(resp.
\overline{H}_{\beta}
and resp.
H_{\beta,h}
), and the action of this finite group is free. Consequently, for
K^{\prime,p}
any normal subgroup of
K^{p}
, we have a tower
\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}^{\prime}}\to\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}^{\prime}}\to\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}^{\prime},h}
associated with
\Phi
and
K^{\prime}=K_{p}K^{\prime,p}
by Construction
4.23
. The quotient of each scheme in the tower by
\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}^{p}
is finite étale.

(3)

The action of
\Delta_{K^{\beta}}(P_{0},G)
on
\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{\beta^{\prime}}},h}
also factors through a finite group, and the action of it on
\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{\beta^{\prime}}},h}
is also free.

Proof.

First, we check the actions factor through finite groups.
To check this action factors through a finite group, we argue as in
[
64
]
. Indeed, it suffices to check over the generic fiber. The action of
(\gamma h\gamma^{-1},\gamma)\in\widetilde{\Delta}_{\beta}
is determined by
\gamma
. There is an almost semi-direct product
Q_{0}\cong G^{*}_{h}\cdot P_{0}
with
G^{*}_{h}
a reductive group over
\mathbb{Q}
which is a finite cover of
Q_{0}/P_{0}
. The group
G^{*}_{h}(\mathbb{Q})
acts on
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi_{\beta^{\prime}}},h}
trivially. Hence, the action of
\Delta_{\beta}
on
\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{\beta^{\prime}}},h}
factors through a finite quotient group by projecting
\widetilde{\Delta}_{\beta}
to
Q_{0}/P_{0}(\mathbb{Q})
. From Construction
4.23
, we see that the action of
\Delta^{\circ}_{K^{\beta}}(P_{0},G)
also factors through a finite group if Part
1
is shown.

By Lemma
1.30
, the group
\Delta_{K^{\beta}}(P_{0},ZP_{\Phi})
is included in
\Delta^{\circ}_{K^{\beta}}(P_{0},G)
. The projection of
\Delta^{\circ}_{K^{\beta}}(P_{0},G)
to
Q_{0}^{\mathrm{ad}}/ZP_{\Phi}^{\mathrm{ad}}(\mathbb{Q})
is an arithmetic subgroup contained in the image of
{}^{\gamma_{\beta}g_{\beta}g_{\alpha}}{K}
. Moreover, this projection is contained in
P_{\Phi}^{\prime\prime}/ZP_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})
(see §
1.2.2
for the notation) by the neatness of
K
and by
[
64
, Thm. 6.19(a)]
. Then the
Q_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})_{+}
-component of
\Delta^{\circ}_{K^{\beta}}(P_{0},G)
centralizes
U_{\Phi}
and is contained in
ZP_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})
by Lemma
1.11
. This proves Part
1
.

Next, we show the freeness of actions.
Let
\overline{x}\in\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{\beta^{\prime}}}}(\kappa)
be a geometric point of
\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{\beta^{\prime}},p}}
, where

\kappa
is an algebraically closed field. Suppose that the action of
\tau=(h,\gamma^{-1})\in\Delta_{K^{\beta}}(P_{0},ZP_{\Phi})
on
\overline{x}
is trivial. The aim is to show that
\tau
acts on every point of
\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{\beta^{\prime}}}}
trivially.
Then
\tau
stabilizes the component
{}^{j}{\mathcal{S}}_{K_{\Phi_{\beta^{\prime}},p}K_{\Phi_{\beta^{\prime}}}^{p}}
containing
\overline{x}
. Up to replacing
K_{\Phi_{\beta^{\prime}},p}
and
K_{p}^{\beta}
with the right conjugate of a representative in
P_{0}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
of
j
, it suffices to assume that
j=1
and to consider the subgroup of
\widetilde{\Delta}_{\beta}
that stabilizes
{}^{1}{\mathcal{S}}_{K_{\Phi_{\beta^{\prime}},p}K_{\Phi_{\beta^{\prime}}}^{p}}
.
This stabilizer is

\begin{split}&\widetilde{\Delta}_{\beta}^{1}:=\{(h_{1},\gamma_{1})\in P_{0}(\mathbb{Q})_{+}P_{0}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})K_{\Phi_{\beta^{\prime}},p}\times Q^{\mathrm{ad}}(\mathbb{Q})^{+}|\gamma_{1}\text{ is lifted to an element }\gamma^{*}_{1}\in Q_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})_{+},\\
&\text{ and }\gamma_{1}^{*}=h_{1}\text{ modulo }{}^{\gamma_{\beta}g_{\beta}g_{\alpha}}{K}\text{ on the right in }G(\mathbb{A}_{f})\}.\end{split}

Modulo the trivial action of
P_{0}(\mathbb{Q})_{+}
, we can assume that
\gamma_{1}
’s in the second component of elements in
\widetilde{\Delta}^{1}_{\beta}
are all lifted to
Q_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})_{+}\cap{}^{\gamma_{\beta}g_{\beta}g_{\alpha}}{K_{p}}
.
Since we can assume
(h,\gamma^{-1})\in\Delta_{K^{\beta}}(P_{0},ZP_{\Phi})
, we assume that
\gamma
can be lifted to
ZP_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})_{+}\cap{}^{\gamma_{\beta}g_{\beta}g_{\alpha}}{K_{p}}
. By Lemma
4.26
below, the action of the last group can be described by twisting constructions for some
G_{0,{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}
as in Section
3
.

Write
G
as an almost-direct product
G\cong(G_{0}/\ker(\pi^{b})\times S_{G})/Z_{G}^{\prime}
, where
S_{G}\subset Z_{G}
and
Z_{G}^{\prime}
is a finite group. Then Lemma
4.26
shows that
\gamma
lifts to
P_{0}^{\dagger}(F)\cap G_{0,{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}(\mathcal{O}_{F,(p)})
, where
P_{0}^{\dagger}:=\pi^{b,-1}(Z_{G}^{\prime})\cdot P_{0}
.

In all cases, we can pull back the
1
-motive with additional structures to
\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{\beta^{\prime}}}}
from the integral model of some Siegel-type boundary mixed Shimura variety.
Suppose that the image of
\beta
is some
\alpha\in I_{G/G_{0}}
. Suppose that
\Phi_{\beta^{\prime}}
maps to
\Phi^{{\ddagger},\prime}\in\mathcal{CLR}(G^{\ddagger},X^{\ddagger})
as in the notation of §
4.2.1
and §
4.2.2
. With the notation in the above-mentioned subsections, in Cases (HS), (STB) and their deep-level cases, we pull back the tautological
1
-motive with additional structures over
\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi^{{\ddagger},\prime}}}
. In Case (
\text{STB}_{n}
) and its deep-level case, we choose an
i
and pull back the tautological family over
\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi^{i}}}
.

By Proposition
3.24
, there is a commutative diagram

(4.18)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{p}}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{F}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V_{\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p}}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}\mathcal{O}_{F,(p)}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V^{p}\mathcal{Q}_{\overline{x}}^{\tau,\mathcal{O}_{F}}}

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\gamma}^{-1}\circ h}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces k\cdot}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{\overline{x}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V^{p}f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{\overline{x}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V^{p}s}

where
s
is induced by an isomorphism between
1
-motives
s:\mathcal{Q}_{\overline{x}}\to\mathcal{Q}^{\tau}_{\overline{x}}
preserving polarizations,
k\in K_{\Phi_{\beta^{\prime}}}^{p}
,
\tilde{\gamma}\in P_{0}^{\dagger}(F)\cap G_{0,{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}(\mathcal{O}_{F,(p)})
is a lifting of
\gamma
and
h\in P_{0}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
.

Since

is in the preimage in

of the image of
{}^{\gamma_{\beta}g_{\beta}g_{\alpha}}{K}
in

,

lies in an open compact subgroup of

.
We replace

with another

-lattice

and replace

with

with a
\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}^{\times}
-isogeny

such that the commutative diagram above induces a commutative diagram preserving polarizations

(4.19)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{p}}^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T^{p}\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{p}}^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T^{p}\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{\overline{x}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces kh^{-1}\tilde{\gamma}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{\overline{x}}}

The automorphism on the right is induced by an automorphism in
\mathrm{Aut}\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}
. Note that we have

\mathrm{Aut}(\mathcal{Q}^{\prime})\subset\mathrm{Aut}(Y_{\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}})\times\mathrm{Aut}(T_{\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}})\times\mathrm{Aut}(A_{\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}}).

Indeed, we can check that
\mathrm{Aut}(\mathcal{Q}^{\prime})\subset\mathrm{Aut}(Y_{\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}})\times\mathrm{Aut}(\mathcal{G}^{\natural}_{\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}})
by definition; and
\mathrm{Aut}(\mathcal{G}^{\natural}_{\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}})\cong\mathrm{Aut}(((\mathcal{Q}^{\prime})^{\vee})^{\circ})\subset\mathrm{Aut}(X_{\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}})\times\mathrm{Aut}(A_{\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}}^{\vee})\cong\mathrm{Aut}(T_{\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}})\times\mathrm{Aut}(A_{\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}})
. Moreover, since the projection to
\mathrm{Aut}(A_{\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}})\to\mathrm{Aut}(T^{p}A_{\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}})
of the right vertical arrow of (
4.19
) is induced by an isomorphism of polarized abelian schemes with level structures, this projection is some multiplication of roots of unity (see, e.g.,
[
44
, Cor. 2.3.3.2]
). We will use these facts later.

Set
ZP_{0}:=Z_{G_{0}}P_{0}
and
W_{0}
the unipotent radical of
P_{0}
. Set
ZW_{0}:=Z_{G_{0}}W_{0}
. We claim that the image of
q:=k^{-1}h\tilde{\gamma}^{-1}
in
ZP_{0}/ZW_{0}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}F)
is trivial. Then it will follow that
q\in ZW_{0}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}F)
. Let us show the claim.

Let
P_{\Phi}^{\dagger}
be the quotient of
P_{0}^{\dagger}
by
\mathbb{K}:=\ker(G_{0}\to G)
. The projection of
q
to
P_{\Phi}^{\dagger}/P_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}F)
via
P_{0}^{\dagger}\to P_{\Phi}^{\dagger}\to P_{\Phi}^{\dagger}/P_{\Phi}
lies in
P_{\Phi}^{\dagger}/P_{\Phi}(F)
, which is a finite group.
Hence, we can replace
q
with
q^{m}
for some positive integer

and assume that
q^{m}
is in
P_{0}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}F)
. The quotient
P_{0}/W_{0}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p}\otimes F)
acts
faithfully
on
\gr_{-1}V_{\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}F
. So, by the diagram (
4.19
) above, the image of
q^{m}
in
P_{0}/W_{0}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}F)
satisfies the equation
X^{M}=1
for some positive integer

, and therefore it is also true for the image of
q
in
ZP_{0}/ZW_{0}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}F)
. But it maps into the image in
ZP_{\Phi}/(Z_{G}W_{0})(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})=ZP_{0}/ZW_{0}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
of a
neat
open compact subgroup
ZP(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})\cap{}^{\gamma_{\beta}g_{\beta}g_{\alpha}}{K}
. Consequently, we have the claim for
q
.

Since
\mathrm{Aut}(\mathcal{Q}^{\prime})\subset\mathrm{Aut}(Y_{\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}})\times\mathrm{Aut}(T_{\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}})\times\mathrm{Aut}(A_{\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}})
,
q
is in
Z_{G_{0}}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}F)
. Now we can follow the argument in
[
36
, Prop. 3.6.4]
and
[
33
, Cor. 4.6.15]
. Since
q\in\mathrm{Aut}(\mathcal{Q}^{\prime})\cap Z_{0}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}F)
, we have
q\in Z_{0,{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}(\mathcal{O}_{F,(p)})
. Replacing
\tilde{\gamma}
with another lifting, we can assume that the prime-to-
p
factor of
q
is trivial.
Then we know that
\tilde{\gamma}\in ZP_{0}(\mathbb{Q})
and
\tilde{\gamma}
maps into
{}^{\gamma_{\beta}g_{\beta}g_{\alpha}}{K_{p}}
in
G(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
. This implies that
\tilde{\gamma}=\gamma\in ZP_{0}(\mathbb{Q})
maps to
K^{\beta}_{p}
. Combining this fact with the paragraph above, we see that

acts on every point of
\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{\beta^{\prime}}}}
trivially, since over the generic fiber,
q
acts as the right action of some element in
K_{\Phi_{\beta^{\prime}},p}
. Note that the last sentence is true because of the condition that
K_{0,p}^{\alpha}=\pi^{-1}({}^{\alpha}{K_{p}})
.

The argument for
\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{K_{\Phi_{\beta^{\prime}}}}
is similar to the argument above. The differences are: for
\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{K_{\Phi_{\beta^{\prime}}}}
, one replaces
V_{?}
with

, and replaces objects related to

-motives
?\mathcal{Q}
with

.

Let us show Part
3
, and the statement in Part
2
for
\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{\beta^{\prime}}},h}
will follow immediately.
The idea is still similar to the paragraphs above with some changes.
We can only assume that
\gamma
lifts to
Q_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})_{+}\cap{}^{\gamma_{\beta}g_{\beta}g_{\alpha}}{K_{p}}
and to
\tilde{\gamma}\in Q_{0}(F)\cap G_{0,{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}(\mathcal{O}_{F,(p)})
. See the definition of
\widetilde{\Delta}^{1}_{\beta}
.

Let
G_{l}
be the smallest connected normal subgroup in
Q_{0}/W_{0}
such that there is an almost-direct product decomposition
Q_{0}/W_{0}=(P_{0}/W_{0})\cdot G_{l}
.
Let
G_{h}^{\prime}:=(Q_{0}/W_{0})/(G_{l}\cdot\mathbb{K})
and
G_{0,h}:=P_{0}/W_{0}
. Then there is a natural central isogeny

, and this isogeny factors through

since

.
Recall that we have fixed an

and
{}^{\gamma_{\beta}g_{\beta}g_{\alpha}}{K_{p}}
is contained in a Bruhat-Tits stabilizer subgroup

of

. The projection to
G_{h}^{\prime}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
of the intersection

is compact and therefore contained in a Bruhat-Tits stabilizer subgroup
K_{\Phi,h,p}^{i,\prime}
. Denote the image of
\gamma
in

by
\overline{\gamma}
. In particular, by Lemma
4.26
, the action of
\overline{\gamma}
on
\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{\beta^{\prime}}},h}
can be described by twisting abelian schemes. Moreover, we can check over generic fiber and by reducedness and separatedness that the action of
\gamma
on
\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{\beta^{\prime}}},h}
is determined by the image
\overline{\gamma}
.
We now choose a finite Galois extension
F
of
\mathbb{Q}
and lift
\overline{\gamma}
to
\tilde{\gamma}_{11}\in G_{0,h,{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}(\mathcal{O}_{F,(p)})
via
G_{0,h}\to G^{\prime}_{h}
(in fact by lifting the projection to
G^{\prime}_{h}(\mathbb{Q})
).

If
(h,\gamma^{-1})
acts on some geometric point of
\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{\beta^{\prime}}},h}
trivially, it follows that the
(-1)
-graded piece of the diagram (
4.19
) is commutative. Denote by
\overline{h}
the projection of

to
G_{0,h}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
. With similar conventions as before, we then see that the projection to

of

is a neat element, where in this case

.
On the other hand,

satisfies the equation

for some positive integer

, as it is a multiplication of a root of unity by
[

, Cor. 2.3.3.2]
and the

-graded piece of (

).
This implies that

again. Finally, by the argument of
[

, Prop. 3.6.4]
and
[

, Cor. 4.6.15]
as above, we see that

, and this implies that

. Up to replacing

with another lifting again, we can assume that the prime-to-
p
factor of

is trivial. Again, this implies that
