acts on every point of
\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{\beta^{\prime}}},h}
trivially.
∎

Note that the action of the whole group
G^{\mathrm{ad}}(\mathbb{Q})_{1}
cannot be described by the twisting construction over integral models. But we have the following statement:

Lemma 4.26
.

With the conventions in the proof above, if
\gamma\in Q_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})^{+}\cap{}^{\gamma_{\beta}g_{\beta}g_{\alpha}}{K_{p}}
, then we can describe the action of
\gamma
as a (left) twisting action.

Proof.

It suffices to deal with Case (STB). Without loss of generality, we assume
\gamma_{\beta}=1
,
g_{\beta}=1
and
g_{\alpha}=1
. We show that the elements in
G(\mathbb{Q})^{+}\cap K_{p}
can be described by twisting.
Write
G\cong(G_{0}/\ker(\pi^{b})\times S_{G})/Z^{\prime}_{G}
as in the last proof, where
S_{G}\subset Z_{G}
, and
Z^{\prime}_{G}
is a finite group.
By assumption,
\gamma
can be projected to
\overline{\gamma}\in G^{\prime}:=G/S_{G}(\mathbb{Q})
, and
\overline{\gamma}
can be lifted to
\tilde{\gamma}\in G_{0}(F)
for some finite Galois extension
F
over
\mathbb{Q}
.

Suppose that
K_{p}=\mathscr{G}_{x}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
is a Bruhat-Tits stabilizer subgroup corresponding to some point
x
in the extended building
\mathcal{B}(G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}},\mathbb{Q}_{p})
.
The point
x
corresponds to a point
y
in the Bruhat-Tits building of
G^{\mathrm{der}}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
, and
y
corresponds to a point
y^{\prime}
in the Bruhat-Tits building of
G^{\prime}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
and a point
y_{0}
in the Bruhat-Tits building of
G_{0,\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
.

Then
\gamma
lies in
\mathscr{G}_{y^{\prime}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
under the projection, where
\mathscr{G}_{y^{\prime}}
is the Bruhat-Tits stabilizer group scheme corresponding to
y^{\prime}
. Let
F\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{Q}_{p}=\prod_{i}F_{v_{i}}
for the places
v_{i}
of
F
over
p
. Then we can view
\gamma
in
\prod_{i}R_{\mathcal{O}_{F_{v_{i}}}/\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathscr{G}_{y^{\prime},\mathcal{O}_{F_{v_{i}}}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
. Moreover,
R_{\mathcal{O}_{F_{v_{i}}}/\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathscr{G}_{y^{\prime},\mathcal{O}_{F_{v_{i}}}}
is the Bruhat-Tits stabilizer group scheme corresponding to the image of
y^{\prime}
in
\mathcal{B}(G_{F_{v_{i}}}^{\prime},F_{v_{i}})\cong\mathcal{B}(R_{F_{v_{i}}/\mathbb{Q}_{p}}G_{F_{v_{i}}}^{\prime},\mathbb{Q}_{p})
. We can also view
\tilde{\gamma}\in G_{0}(F)
as an element in
R_{F\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{Q}_{p}/\mathbb{Q}_{p}}G_{0}
. Since the map
R_{F\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{Q}_{p}/\mathbb{Q}_{p}}G_{0}\to R_{F\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{Q}_{p}/\mathbb{Q}_{p}}G^{\prime}
is a central isogeny between reductive groups,
\tilde{\gamma}\in\prod_{i}R_{\mathcal{O}_{F_{v_{i}}}/\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathscr{G}_{y_{0},\mathcal{O}_{F_{v_{i}}}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\cap G_{0}(F)\cong\mathscr{G}_{y_{0}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{F})\cap G_{0}(F)
.
Hence,
\tilde{\gamma}
lies in
G_{0,{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{F})\cap G_{0}(F)\subset G_{0,{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}}({\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{F})
, as desired.
∎

When
(G_{0},X_{0})=(G,X_{b})
and when
\gamma_{\beta}=g_{\alpha}=1
, we have reproved (and generalized) a result due to Madapusi.

Corollary 4.27

(cf. the proof of
[
50
, Thm. 5.3.1]
)
.

The quotient map

\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi},h}\to\Delta_{\Phi,K}\backslash\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi},h}

is finite étale.

Remark 4.28
.

Note that we show this without assumptions on
K_{p}=K_{0,p}
.
In fact, Madapusi has already remarked in the proof of
loc. cit.
that one can use Kisin’s theory of twisting abelian varieties to study the action of
\Delta_{\Phi,K}
, and has also remarked that one can drop the hyperspecial assumption added in
loc. cit.
by doing this.

The lemma above also generalizes the argument in the proof of
[
33
, Cor. 4.6.15]
; in particular, we have shown the following result:

Proposition 4.29
.

When
P_{0}
is a normal subgroup of
G_{0}
and when
ZP_{\Phi}=G
, Part
2
of Lemma
4.25
implies that the morphism
\mathcal{S}_{K_{0}^{\alpha}}\to\mathcal{S}_{K}
induced by taking the quotient of
{\Delta}_{{}^{\alpha}{K}}(G_{0},G)
is finite étale and that
\{\mathcal{S}_{K_{p}K^{p}}\}_{K^{p}}
has finite étale transition maps. Taking inverse limits with finite étale transition maps, the quotient map
\mathcal{S}_{K_{0,p}^{\alpha}}\to\mathcal{S}_{K_{p}}
induced by taking the quotient of
\Delta_{{}^{\alpha}{K_{p}}}(G_{0},G)
is pro-finite étale.

Now, we see that the tower associated with
\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}}
has the desired structure.

Proposition 4.30
.

Under the conventions and assumptions above, we have the following statements for different cases:

(1)

In all cases,
\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}}
is a torus torsor over
\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}}
under
\mathbf{E}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}}
(see the paragraph below Definition
1.48
).
Moreover, in Cases (HS) and (
\mathrm{STB}_{n}
),

\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}}
is an abelian scheme torsor over
\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi},h}
.

(2)

In all cases, the action of
\Delta^{ZP,\circ}_{\Phi,K}
on
\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}}
is trivial.

(3)

In Case (HS), the inverse limit formed by
\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi,p}}:=\varprojlim_{\widetilde{K}^{p}_{\Phi}}\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}}
satisfies the extension property.

Proof.

By
[
64
, 3.13]
and Lemma
1.14
, we know that
\mathrm{Sh}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}}\to\overline{\mathrm{Sh}}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}}
is a torsor under
\mathbf{E}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}}
. By
[
50
, Thm. 4.1.5 (4)]
,
\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{\beta^{\prime}}}}\to\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{K_{\Phi_{\beta^{\prime}}}}
is an
\mathbf{E}_{K_{\Phi_{\beta^{\prime}}}}
-torsor. From the construction of
\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}}\to\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}}
as a quotient, we have a finite morphism
\coprod_{\beta\in I_{\Phi}}\mathbf{E}_{K_{\Phi_{\beta^{\prime}}}}\times\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{\beta^{\prime}}}}\to\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}}\times_{\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}}}\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}}
. From Lemma
4.25
(2), we see that the target of this morphism is normal. This morphism factors as
\coprod_{\beta\in I_{\Phi}}\mathbf{E}_{K_{\Phi_{\beta^{\prime}}}}\times\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{\beta^{\prime}}}}\to\mathbf{E}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}}\times\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}}\to\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}}\times_{\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}}}\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}}
, and the last morphism
\mathbf{E}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}}\times\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}}\to\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}}\times_{\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}}}\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}}
is an isomorphism by Zariski’s main theorem. Finally, since, over the fppf cover
\coprod_{\beta\in I_{\Phi}}\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{\beta^{\prime}}}}\to\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}}
,
\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}}\to\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}}
admits a section, we conclude that it is an
\mathbf{E}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}}
-torsor.
The claim for abelian scheme torsors is proved in a similar manner. By
[
50
, Thm. 4.1.5 (1)]
,
\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{K_{\Phi_{\beta^{\prime}}}}\to\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{\beta^{\prime}}},h}
is a torsor under some abelian scheme
\mathcal{A}_{K_{\Phi_{\beta^{\prime}}}}
over
\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{\beta^{\prime}}},h}
. By
[
64
, 3.13]
again,
\overline{\mathrm{Sh}}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}}\to\mathrm{Sh}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi},h}
is a torsor under some abelian scheme
A_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}}
over
\mathrm{Sh}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi},h}
, and
\overline{\mathrm{Sh}}_{K_{\Phi_{\beta^{\prime}}}}\to\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi_{\beta^{\prime}}},h}
is a torsor under
A_{K_{\Phi_{\beta^{\prime}}}}
, where
A_{K_{\Phi_{\beta^{\prime}}}}:=\mathcal{A}_{K_{\Phi_{\beta^{\prime}}}}\times_{\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{\beta^{\prime}}},h}}\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi_{\beta^{\prime}}},h}
is an abelian scheme over
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi_{\beta^{\prime}}},h}
.
By Lemma
4.25
(2),
\coprod_{\beta\in I_{\Phi}}\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{K_{\Phi_{\beta^{\prime}}}}\to\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}}
and
\coprod_{\beta\in I_{\Phi}}\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{\beta^{\prime}}},h}\to\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi},h}
are finite étale surjective morphisms between normal schemes. Then the induced morphism
\coprod_{\beta\in I_{\Phi}}\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{K_{\Phi_{\beta^{\prime}}}}\to\coprod_{\beta\in I_{\Phi}}\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{\beta^{\prime}}},h}\times_{\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi},h}}\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}}
is finite étale and surjective. Let
\mathcal{K}
be the schematic closure in
\mathcal{A}:=\coprod_{\beta\in I_{\Phi}}\mathcal{A}_{K_{\Phi_{\beta^{\prime}}}}
of the kernel of
\coprod_{\beta\in I_{\Phi}}A_{K_{\Phi_{\beta^{\prime}}}}\to A_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}}
. Then
\mathcal{K}
is finite over
\coprod_{\beta\in I_{\Phi}}\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{\beta^{\prime}}},h}
as it is a closed subscheme in
\mathcal{A}[n]
for some positive integer
n
.
By Zariski’s main theorem, the quotient of
\coprod_{\beta\in I_{\Phi}}\overline{S}_{K_{\Phi_{\beta^{\prime}}}}
by
\mathcal{K}
is isomorphic to
\coprod_{\beta\in I_{\Phi}}\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{\beta^{\prime}}},h}\times_{\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi},h}}\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}}
. Hence,
\mathcal{K}
is a finite étale group scheme over
\coprod_{\beta\in I_{\Phi}}\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{\beta^{\prime}}},h}
. We then have that
\mathcal{A}/\mathcal{K}
is an abelian scheme over
\coprod_{\beta\in I_{\Phi}}\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{\beta^{\prime}}},h}
extending
A_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}}
. By Lemma
4.25
(2) again,
\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}}
is an abelian scheme torsor over
\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi},h}
since we have checked it over an étale cover
\coprod_{\beta\in I_{\Phi}}\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{\beta^{\prime}}},h}
of
\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi},h}
.

Now let us show Part
2
. We argue as in
[
64
, 7.3]
and the proof of Lemma
4.25
(1).
The projection of
\Delta_{\Phi,K}^{ZP,\circ}
to
Q_{\Phi}/ZP_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})
is a neat arithmetic subgroup. By
[
64
, Thm. 6.19(a)]
and Lemma
1.11
, this projection is in a neat arithmetic subgroup in
P^{\prime\prime}_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})
, so it has to be in
ZP_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})
, as desired.

Now assume that we are in Case (HS). By
[
49
, Lem. 2.1.3]
and Proposition
1.23
, we can and we will check the extension property over
\mathcal{O}^{p}:=\mathcal{O}_{E^{p}}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{E_{0}}}\mathcal{O}_{E_{0},(v)}
, where
E^{p}
is the maximal field extension of
E^{\prime}
that is unramified at all primes dividing
p
.
It follows from essentially the same argument as in
[
37
, E. 6]
.
By
[
50
, A.3.5 and A.3.6]
, we have that,
for any rational prime
l\neq p
and any neat open compact subgroup
K_{\Phi_{0}}^{p,l}\subset P_{\Phi_{0}}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p,l})
,

\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{0},p}K^{p,l}_{\Phi_{0}}}
has the extension property.
Part
3
will follow from
[
49
, Lem 2.1.5 and Lem. 2.1.4]
and Lemma
4.25
, if we show that the quotient of
\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{0},p}K_{\Phi_{0}}^{p,l},\mathcal{O}^{p}}
by
\Delta_{K^{\beta,l}}
factors through a finite group, where

\Delta_{K^{\beta,l}}:=\ker(\mathscr{A}(P_{0})\to\mathscr{A}(ZP_{\Phi})/K^{\beta,l}),

K^{\beta,l}:=ZP_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f})\cap{}^{\gamma_{\beta}g_{\beta}g_{\alpha}}{K^{l}}
and
K^{l}=K_{p}K^{l,p}
for some neat compact open
K^{l,p}\subset G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p,l})
.

Write
\mathscr{A}(P_{0})
as
P_{0}(\mathbb{A}_{f})\cdot\mathscr{A}^{\circ}(P_{0})
and
\mathscr{A}(ZP_{\Phi})
as
ZP_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f})\cdot\mathscr{A}^{\circ}(ZP_{\Phi})
. Since

\ker(P_{0}(\mathbb{Q})_{+}^{-}\backslash P_{0}(\mathbb{A}_{f})/K_{\Phi_{0},p}K_{\Phi_{0}}^{p,l}\to ZP_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})_{+}^{-}\backslash ZP_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f})/K^{\beta,l})

is finite (since the kernel of
P_{0}\to ZP_{\Phi}
is finite by construction, one can see this by decomposing to
l
- and away-from-
l
places), and by Lemma
4.5
,
the quotient of
\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{0},p}K_{\Phi_{0}}^{p,l},\mathcal{O}^{p}}
by
\Delta_{K^{\beta,l}}
indeed factors through a finite group.
∎

Construction 4.31
.

By Proposition
4.30
,
\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}}\to\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}}
is an
\mathbf{E}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}}
-torsor. Let
\Sigma
be an admissible cone decomposition and
\sigma\in\Sigma(\Phi)
such that
\sigma^{\circ}\subset\mathbf{P}^{+}_{\Phi}
. It makes sense to define

\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}}(\Sigma):=\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}}\times^{\mathbf{E}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}}}\mathbf{E}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}}(\Sigma),

where
\mathbf{E}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}}(\Sigma)
is the affine torus embedding of
\mathbf{E}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}}
defined by
\Sigma^{+}(\Phi)
,

\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}}(\sigma):=\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}}\times^{\mathbf{E}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}}}\mathbf{E}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}}(\sigma),

and the
\sigma
-stratum of
\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}}(\sigma)
:

\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi},\sigma}:=\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}}\times^{\mathbf{E}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}}}\mathbf{E}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi},\sigma}.

Note that there is a canonical projection from
\mathbf{E}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}}
to
\mathbf{E}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi},\sigma}
.
\square

Finally, let us explicitly check the following statement:

Proposition 4.32
.

With the assumptions and constructions above, the isomorphism
\mathrm{Z}_{[ZP^{b}({\Phi},\sigma)],K}\cong\Delta^{ZP,\circ}_{\Phi,K}\backslash\mathrm{Sh}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi},\sigma}
extends to

(4.20)

\mathcal{Z}_{[ZP^{b}({\Phi},\sigma)],K}\cong\Delta^{ZP,\circ}_{\Phi,K}\backslash\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi},\sigma}.

Moreover, this isomorphism extends to an isomorphism

(4.21)

{(\mathcal{S}^{\Sigma}_{K})}{}^{\wedge{\ }}_{\mathcal{Z}_{[ZP^{b}({\Phi},\sigma)],K}}\cong\Delta^{ZP,\circ}_{\Phi,K}\backslash{(\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}}(\sigma))}{}^{\wedge{\ }}_{\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi},\sigma}}.

Since we assume that
K
is neat, the quotients of
\Delta^{ZP,\circ}_{\Phi,K}
in the right-hand sides of (
4.20
) and (
4.21
) are isomorphic to
\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi},\sigma}
and
{(\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}}(\sigma))}{}^{\wedge{\ }}_{\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi},\sigma}}
, respectively.

Proof.

The last sentence follows from Proposition
4.30
(2).
Write the RHS of (
4.20
) as
\Delta_{\Phi,K}^{ZP}\backslash\Delta_{\Phi,K}^{ZP}\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi},\sigma}\cong G(\mathbb{Q})_{+}\backslash G(\mathbb{Q})_{+}\times^{\widetilde{\Delta}_{\Phi,K}^{ZP}\cap G(\mathbb{Q})_{+}}\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi},\sigma}
. We write the last term as
G(\mathbb{Q})_{+}\backslash G(\mathbb{Q})_{+}\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi},\sigma}.

From the construction of
\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}}
, we have

\begin{split}&G(\mathbb{Q})_{+}\backslash G(\mathbb{Q})_{+}\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi},\sigma}\\
&\cong G(\mathbb{Q})_{+}\backslash G(\mathbb{Q})_{+}\coprod_{\beta\in I_{\Phi}}\gamma_{\beta}\cdot\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{\beta}},\sigma_{\beta}}/\Delta_{K^{\beta}}(P_{0},ZP_{\Phi})\\
&\cong\coprod_{\overline{\beta}\in J_{\Phi}}G(\mathbb{Q})_{+}\backslash G(\mathbb{Q})_{+}\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{\beta^{\prime}}},\sigma_{\beta^{\prime}}}\\
&\cong\coprod_{\alpha\in I_{G/G_{0}}}\coprod_{\pi(g_{0}^{\alpha})\alpha\sim g^{b}}G(\mathbb{Q})_{+}\backslash G(\mathbb{Q})_{+}\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{0}^{\alpha}},\sigma_{0}^{\alpha}}\\
&\cong\coprod_{\alpha\in I_{G/G_{0}}}\coprod_{\pi(g_{0}^{\alpha})\alpha\sim g^{b}}\Delta_{{}^{g_{0}^{\alpha}g_{\alpha}}{K}}(P_{0},G)\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{0}^{\alpha}},\sigma_{0}^{\alpha}}/\Delta_{{}^{g_{0}^{\alpha}g_{\alpha}}{K}}(P_{0},G)\\
&\cong\coprod_{\alpha\in I_{G/G_{0}}}\coprod_{\pi(g_{0}^{\alpha})\alpha\sim g^{b}}(\Delta_{{}^{\alpha}{K}}(G_{0},G)\mathcal{Z}_{[(\Phi_{0}^{\alpha},\sigma_{0}^{\alpha})],K_{0}^{\alpha}})/\Delta_{{}^{\alpha}{K}}(G_{0},G)\\
&\cong\mathrm{LHS}.\end{split}

In the equations above, we write
\Phi_{\beta^{\prime}}=\gamma_{\beta}\cdot\Phi_{\beta}
and
\sigma_{\beta^{\prime}}:=\gamma_{\beta}\cdot\sigma_{\beta}
. Write
G(\mathbb{Q})_{+}\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{\beta^{\prime}}},\sigma_{\beta^{\prime}}}:=G(\mathbb{Q})_{+}\times^{\Delta_{K^{\beta}}(P_{0},G)}\Delta_{K^{\beta}}(P_{0},G)\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{\beta^{\prime}}},\sigma_{\beta^{\prime}}}
, where
\Delta_{K^{\beta}}(P_{0},G):=\ker(\mathscr{A}(P_{0})\times^{P_{0}^{\mathrm{ad}}(\mathbb{Q})^{+}}Q_{0}^{\mathrm{ad}}(\mathbb{Q})^{+}\to\mathscr{A}(G)/{}^{\gamma_{\beta}g_{\beta}g_{\alpha}}{K})
(see the beginning of §
4.2.7
for the definition of
Q_{0}^{\mathrm{ad}}(\mathbb{Q})^{+}
). Similarly, we write
G(\mathbb{Q})_{+}\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{0}^{\alpha}},\sigma_{0}^{\alpha}}:=G(\mathbb{Q})_{+}\times^{\Delta_{{}^{g_{0}^{\alpha}g_{\alpha}}{K}}(P_{0},G)}\Delta_{{}^{g_{0}^{\alpha}g_{\alpha}}{K}}(P_{0},G)\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{0}^{\alpha}},\sigma_{0}^{\alpha}}
, where
\Delta_{{}^{g_{0}^{\alpha}g_{\alpha}}{K}}(P_{0},G):=\ker(\mathscr{A}(P_{0})\times^{P_{0}^{\mathrm{ad}}(\mathbb{Q})^{+}}Q_{0}^{\mathrm{ad}}(\mathbb{Q})^{+}\to\mathscr{A}(G)/{}^{g_{0}^{\alpha}g_{\alpha}}{K})
.

Let us explain the equations above: The second line comes from the definition. Any
(p,\gamma^{-1})\in\Delta_{K^{\beta}}(P_{0},ZP_{\Phi})
is determined by the element
\gamma
that is liftable to
ZP_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})_{+}/Z(\mathbb{Q})
and to
\gamma^{*}\in ZP_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})_{+}
. Then
\Delta_{K^{\beta}}(P_{0},ZP_{\Phi})\subset\Delta_{K^{\beta}}(P_{0},G)
.
We see the third line is isomorphic to

\coprod_{\overline{\beta}\in J_{\Phi}}G(\mathbb{Q})_{+}\backslash G(\mathbb{Q})_{+}(\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{\beta^{\prime}}},\sigma_{\beta^{\prime}}}/\Delta_{K^{\beta}}(P_{0},ZP_{\Phi})).

Moreover, the stabilizer in
\widetilde{\Delta}^{ZP}_{\Phi,K}\cap G(\mathbb{Q})_{+}
of an element in
J_{\Phi}
is isomorphic to
\mathrm{Stab}_{G(\mathbb{Q})_{+}}(D_{\Phi_{\beta}})\cap\pi(P_{0}(\mathbb{A}_{f})){}^{\gamma_{\beta}g_{\beta}g_{\alpha}}{K}
; the image of
\Delta_{K^{\beta}}(P_{0},G)
, viewed as elements in
(p,\gamma)\in P_{0}(\mathbb{A}_{f})\times G(\mathbb{Q})_{+}
, is surjective to this stabilizer. Hence, we have the isomorphism from the second line to the third line.
The fourth line comes from the discussion in Lemma
4.18
, and the fifth line is the definition. The isomorphism from the fifth line to the sixth line follows from the fact that
\Delta_{\Phi_{0}^{\alpha},K_{0}^{\alpha}}
maps to
\Delta_{{}^{g_{0}^{\alpha}g_{\alpha}}{K}}(P_{0},G)
and the fact that
\Delta_{{}^{g_{0}^{\alpha}g_{\alpha}}{K}}(P_{0},G)
is contained in
\Delta_{{}^{\alpha}{K}}(G_{0},G)
via the map sending
(h,\gamma)
to
((g_{0}^{\alpha})^{-1}hg_{0}^{\alpha},\gamma)
; in addition, an element
((g_{0}^{\alpha})^{-1}hg_{0}^{\alpha},\gamma)\in\Delta_{{}^{\alpha}{K}}(G_{0},G)
stabilizes
\mathcal{Z}_{[(\Phi_{0}^{\alpha},\sigma_{0}^{\alpha})]}
if and only if it is in the image of
\Delta_{{}^{g_{0}^{\alpha}g_{\alpha}}{K}}(P_{0},G)
.

Let us show the second isomorphism. Note that we have similar isomorphisms as above if we replace closed strata with the corresponding twisted affine torus embeddings. Hence, we have

\begin{split}&\mathrm{RHS}\\
&\cong\coprod_{\alpha\in I_{G/G_{0}}}\coprod_{\pi(g_{0}^{\alpha})\alpha\sim g^{b}}(\Delta_{{}^{\alpha}{K}}(G_{0},G){(\mathcal{S}_{K_{0}^{\alpha}}(\sigma_{0}^{\alpha}))}{}^{\wedge{\ }}_{\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{0}^{\alpha}},\sigma_{0}^{\alpha}}})/\Delta_{{}^{\alpha}{K}}(G_{0},G)\\
&\cong\coprod_{\alpha\in I_{G/G_{0}}}\coprod_{\pi(g_{0}^{\alpha})\alpha\sim g^{b}}(\Delta_{{}^{\alpha}{K}}(G_{0},G){(\mathcal{S}_{K_{0}^{\alpha}}^{\Sigma_{0}^{\alpha}})}{}^{\wedge{\ }}_{\mathcal{Z}_{[(\Phi_{0}^{\alpha},\sigma_{0}^{\alpha})],K_{0}^{\alpha}}})/\Delta_{{}^{\alpha}{K}}(G_{0},G)\\
&\cong\mathrm{LHS}.\end{split}

The third line to the last line requires that the actions of
\Delta_{{}^{\alpha}{K}}(G_{0},G)
on boundaries and on the compactification exist and are compatible, which follows from Lemma
4.10
and
[
50
, Lem. A.3.4]
.
∎

4.2.8.

Assume that we are in Case (
\text{STB}_{n}
) or (HS). From
\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}^{b}}(G,X_{b})
, we can switch it to
\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}^{a}}:=\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}^{a}}(G,X_{a})
by multiplying the Galois action induced by
c
.

Construction 4.33
.

Let
\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi,p},\mathcal{O}^{\prime,ur}_{(v)}}:=\varprojlim_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}^{p}\text{\ neat open compact}}\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi},\mathcal{O}^{\prime,ur}_{(v)}}
. Similarly, define
\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi,p},\mathcal{O}^{\prime,ur}_{(v)}}
and
\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi,h,p},\mathcal{O}^{\prime,ur}_{(v)}}
.

Recall that
Z:=Z_{G}
. Let
c
be the homomorphism
c:\mathbb{S}\to Z_{\mathbb{R}}
defined as in §
1.4.3
. Then
(Z,\{c\})
is a
0
-dimensional Shimura datum.
Let
r_{E^{\prime},{K_{Z,p}K_{Z}^{p}}}(Z,\{c\})
be the homomorphism

\mathrm{Gal}(\overline{E}^{\prime}/E^{\prime})\to\mathrm{Gal}(E^{\prime,ab}/E^{\prime})\to Z(\mathbb{Q})\backslash Z(\mathbb{A}_{f})/K_{Z,p}K_{Z}^{p}
defined as in §
1.1.2
describing the action of
\mathrm{Gal}(\overline{E}^{\prime}/E^{\prime})
on
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{Z,p}K_{Z}^{p},\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}\cong\pi_{0}(\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{Z,p}K_{Z}^{p},\overline{\mathbb{Q}}})\cong Z(\mathbb{Q})\backslash Z(\mathbb{A}_{f})/K_{Z,p}K_{Z}^{p}
. Here we can take
K_{Z,p}
as
K_{p}\cap Z(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
and take
K_{Z}^{p}\subset K^{p}\cap Z(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
.
By Proposition
4.24
, the left action of
Z(\mathbb{A}_{f})
on
\mathrm{Sh}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}}
extends to an action on
\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}}
, and
Z(\mathbb{Q})K_{Z,p}K_{Z}^{p}
is in the kernel of this action since it is so over the generic fiber.

Then we can define a new Galois action as follows:
Let
r_{b}:\mathrm{Gal}(E^{\prime,ab}/E^{\prime})\times\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi,p},\mathcal{O}^{\prime,ur}_{(v)}}\to\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi,p},\mathcal{O}^{\prime,ur}_{(v)}}
be the action of
\mathrm{Gal}(E^{\prime,ab}/E^{\prime})
on
\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi,p},\mathcal{O}^{\prime,ur}_{(v)}}
which determines the descent datum of
\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi,p},\mathcal{O}^{\prime,ur}_{(v)}}
from
\mathcal{O}^{\prime,ur}_{(v)}
to
\mathcal{O}^{\prime}_{(v)}
. Define
r_{a}:\mathrm{Gal}(E^{\prime,ab}/E^{\prime})\times\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi,p},\mathcal{O}^{\prime,ur}_{(v)}}\to\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi,p},\mathcal{O}^{\prime,ur}_{(v)}}
by sending each
\sigma\in\mathrm{Gal}(E^{\prime,ab}/E^{\prime})
to
r_{a}(\sigma):=r_{E^{\prime},{K_{Z,p}K_{Z}^{p}}}(Z,\{c\})(\sigma)\circ r_{b}(\sigma)=r_{b}(\sigma)\circ r_{E^{\prime},{K_{Z,p}K_{Z}^{p}}}(Z,\{c\})(\sigma)
. We can exchange the order since the left action of
Z(\mathbb{A}_{f})
is defined over
\mathcal{O}_{(v)}^{\prime}
. Note that the descent datum factors through
\mathrm{Gal}(E^{p}/E^{\prime})
since the intersection of
n
quasi-parahoric subgroups in
G(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
still contains the parahoric subgroup of
Z(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
.

The tower induced by the new descent datum determined by
r_{a}
is a model of
\mathrm{Sh}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi,p}^{a}}:=\mathrm{Sh}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi,p}^{a}}(G,X_{a})
over
\mathcal{O}_{(v)}^{\prime}
, we denote it by
\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}^{a}_{\Phi,p}}
or
\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}^{a}_{\Phi,p}}(G,X_{a})
. Similarly, we define the towers of
\mathcal{O}_{(v)}^{\prime}
-schemes
\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{\widetilde{K}^{a}_{\Phi,p}}
and
\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}^{a}_{\Phi,p},h}
which are models of
\overline{\mathrm{Sh}}_{\widetilde{K}^{a}_{\Phi,p}}
and
\mathrm{Sh}_{\widetilde{K}^{a}_{\Phi,p},h}
, respectively.

By étale descent of morphisms and since the schemes involved are normal and separated, we see that for any neat open compact
K^{p}\subset G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
, there is also a tower of
\mathcal{O}^{\prime}_{(v)}
-schemes
\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}^{a}}\to\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}^{a}}\to\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}^{a},h}
. Also from étale descent, we know that this tower also satisfies Proposition
4.30
and Proposition
4.24
.

\square

Remark 4.34
.

It is possible to write the tower
\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}^{i}_{\Phi,p},\mathcal{O}^{\prime,ur}_{(v)}}
in a similar form as
[
33
, Cor. 4.6.15]
, for
i=a,b
and for every
\Phi
. However, the complexity will increase when one wants to study the relation between the compactifications and the boundaries with this method. This partially explains why we chose to use a different strategy here to study compactifications.

4.3.
Main theorem on toroidal compactifications

4.3.1.

For the rest of this section, let us complete the main constructions.

Let us fix an open compact subgroup
K_{2,p}\subset G_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
and a neat open compact subgroup
K_{2}^{p}
of
G_{2}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
. Let
\Sigma_{2}
be an admissible rational polyhedral cone decomposition of
(G_{2},X_{2})
. By Proposition
1.35
, Corollary
1.37
and Proposition
1.47
, there is a neat open compact subgroup
K=K_{p}K^{p}
of
G(\mathbb{A}_{f})
such that,
K_{p}\cap G_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})=K_{2,p}
, and such that
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{2}}\to\mathrm{Sh}_{K}
is an open and closed embedding; moreover, there is a refinement
\Sigma_{2}^{\prime}
of
\Sigma_{2}
and a
ZP
-invariant admissible cone decomposition
\Sigma
associated with
(G,X_{a},K)
and
(G,X_{b},K)
, such that
\Sigma_{2}^{\prime}
is induced by
\Sigma
, and such that the morphisms between toroidal compactifications, boundary mixed Shimura varieties and boundary strata from the ones associated with
(G_{2},X_{2},K_{2})
to those associated with
(G,X_{a},K)
are all open and closed embeddings. For different cases (HS), (
\text{STB}_{n}
) and (DL), we choose
(G_{0},X_{0})
,
(G,X)
,
g_{\alpha}
,
K=K_{p}K^{p}
,
(G^{{\ddagger}},X^{{\ddagger}})
,
K_{0}^{\alpha}=K_{0,p}^{\alpha}K_{0}^{\alpha,p}
,
K^{{\ddagger},\alpha}=K_{p}^{{\ddagger},\alpha}K^{{\ddagger},\alpha,p}
, etc., according to the discussion at the beginning of §
4.2
. By Proposition
1.47
,
\Sigma
can be and will be chosen such that
\Sigma
also induces
\Sigma_{0}^{\alpha}
by pulling back under
\pi^{b}(g_{\alpha})
, and such that good toroidal compactifications
\mathcal{S}_{K_{0}^{\alpha}}^{\Sigma_{0}^{\alpha}}
associated with
(G_{0},X_{0},K_{0}^{\alpha},\Sigma_{0}^{\alpha})
satisfying
[
50
, Thm. 4.1.5]
can be constructed.

Let us construct integral models of toroidal compactifications of
(G,X_{a})
and
(G_{2},X_{2})
.

Construction 4.35
.

Let
\Sigma
and
\Sigma_{0}^{\alpha}
be cone decompositions chosen as mentioned in Construction
4.16
and at the beginning of §
4.3.1
. Let
\Phi_{2}
be a cusp label representative of
(G_{2},X_{2})
that maps to
\Phi\in\mathcal{CLR}(G,X_{a})
under
\pi^{a}
. By §
1.4
, we can view
\Phi
as a cusp label representative of
(G,X_{b})
in
\mathcal{CLR}(G,X_{b})
.

Let us first treat cases (HS) and (
\text{STB}_{n}
).

We construct
\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}^{a}}\to\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}^{a}}\to\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}^{a},h}
as in Construction
4.33
.
Since
\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}^{a}_{\Phi}}\to\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{\widetilde{K}^{a}_{\Phi}}
is an
\mathbf{E}_{K_{\Phi}}
-torsor, we define
\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}^{a}}(\Sigma)
,
\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}^{a}}(\sigma)
and
\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}^{a},\sigma}
as before. More precisely,
let
\Sigma
be an admissible cone decomposition and
\sigma\in\Sigma(\Phi)
such that
\sigma^{\circ}\subset\mathbf{P}^{+}_{\Phi}
. Define

\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}^{a}_{\Phi}}(\Sigma):=\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}^{a}_{\Phi}}\times^{\mathbf{E}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}}}\mathbf{E}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}}(\Sigma),

where
\mathbf{E}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}}(\Sigma)
is the affine torus embedding of
\mathbf{E}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}}
defined by
\Sigma^{+}(\Phi)
,

\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}^{a}_{\Phi}}(\sigma):=\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}^{a}_{\Phi}}\times^{\mathbf{E}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}}}\mathbf{E}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}}(\sigma),

and the
\sigma
-stratum of
\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}^{a}_{\Phi}}(\sigma)
:

\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}^{a}_{\Phi},\sigma}:=\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}^{a}_{\Phi}}\times^{\mathbf{E}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}}}\mathbf{E}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi},\sigma}.

Let
\sigma_{2}\in\Sigma_{2}^{\prime}(\Phi_{2})
be the cone such that
\sigma_{2}^{\circ}
maps to
\sigma^{\circ}
. Let
\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{2}}}(\sigma_{2})
be the normalization of
\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}^{a}}(\sigma)
in
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi_{2}}}(\sigma_{2})
via the composition of morphisms
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi_{2}}}(\sigma_{2})\to\mathrm{Sh}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}^{a}}(\sigma)\hookrightarrow\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}^{a}}(\sigma)
. We define
\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{2}},\sigma_{2}}
,
\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{K_{\Phi_{2}}}
and
\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{2}},h}
similarly. Hence, this construction induces towers of schemes
\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{2}}}(\sigma_{2})\to\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{K_{\Phi_{2}}}\to\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{2}},h}
, and
\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{2}},\sigma_{2}}\to\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{K_{\Phi_{2}}}\to\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{2}},h}
.
\square

For all the cases, we can continue with the following construction:

Construction 4.36
.

Let
K_{2,p}
be an open compact subgroup contained in
K_{2,p}^{\prime}\subset G_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
, where
K_{2,p}^{\prime}
is an intersection of
n
Bruhat-Tits stabilizer subgroups. Then
K_{2,p}^{\prime}
is contained in
K^{\prime}_{p}\subset G(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
, where
K^{\prime}_{p}
is an intersection of
n
Bruhat-Tits stabilizer subgroups of
G(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
. Set
K_{2}:=K_{2,p}K^{p}_{2}
and
K^{\prime}:=K_{p}^{\prime}K^{\prime,p}
where
K^{p}_{2}\subset K^{\prime,p}
are neat open compact.

(1)

Let
\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{2}}}
(resp.
\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{K_{\Phi_{2}}}
, and resp.
\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{2},h}}
) be the normalization of
\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}^{\prime,a}_{\Phi}}
(resp.
\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{\widetilde{K}^{\prime,a}_{\Phi}}
and resp.
\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}^{\prime,a}_{\Phi},h}
) in
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi_{2}}}
(resp.
\overline{\mathrm{Sh}}_{K_{\Phi_{2}}}
, and resp.
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi_{2},h}}
).

(2)

Let
\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}^{a}_{\Phi}}
(resp.
\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{\widetilde{K}^{a}_{\Phi}}
and resp.
\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}^{a}_{\Phi},h}
) for (DL) be the normalization of
\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}^{\prime,a}_{\Phi}}
(resp.
\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{\widetilde{K}^{\prime,a}_{\Phi}}
and resp.
\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}^{\prime,a}_{\Phi},h}
) in (HS) or (
\text{STB}_{n}
) case in
\mathrm{Sh}_{\widetilde{K}^{a}_{\Phi}}
(resp.
\overline{\mathrm{Sh}}_{\widetilde{K}^{a}_{\Phi}}
and resp.
\mathrm{Sh}_{\widetilde{K}^{a}_{\Phi},h}
).

(3)

Recall that
F_{K_{Z}}
is a number field such that every geometrically connected component of
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{Z}}
is defined over
F_{K_{Z}}
, where
K_{Z}
is an open compact subgroup of
Z(\mathbb{A}_{f})
contained in
K
and
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{Z}}:=\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{Z}}(Z,\{c\})
(see §
1.4.3
). We denote
E_{K_{Z}}^{\prime}:=E^{\prime}\cdot F_{K_{Z}}
and choose
F_{K_{Z}}
such that
E_{K_{Z}}^{\prime}/E^{\prime}
is finite and Galois. Note that
E^{\prime}_{K_{Z}}
can not be chosen to be unramified over
E^{\prime}
at
p
in general. We can still find a
F_{K_{Z}}
so that
E^{\prime}_{K_{Z}}
is unramified over
E^{\prime}
at
p
if
K_{p}
contains the parahoric subgroup of
Z(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
.
Let
\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}:=\mathcal{O}_{E^{\prime}_{K_{Z}}}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{E^{\prime}}}\mathcal{O}^{\prime}_{(v)}
.
Let
\mathcal{S}^{\Sigma_{2}^{\prime}}_{K_{2},\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}
be the normalization of
\mathcal{S}_{K,\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}^{\Sigma}
in
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{2},E^{\prime}_{K_{Z}}}^{\Sigma_{2}^{\prime}}
via the composition of morphisms
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{2},E^{\prime}_{K_{Z}}}^{\Sigma_{2}^{\prime}}\to\mathrm{Sh}_{K}^{\Sigma}(G,X_{a})_{E^{\prime}_{K_{Z}}}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathrm{Sh}^{\Sigma}_{K}(G,X_{b})_{E^{\prime}_{K_{Z}}}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{S}_{K,\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}^{\Sigma}
, where the last subscript
\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}
denotes the normalized base change (i.e., the normalization of the base change) of
\mathcal{S}^{\Sigma}_{K}
from
\mathcal{O}_{(v)}^{\prime}
to
\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}
. The middle isomorphism is from Corollary
1.44
. Then
\mathcal{S}^{\Sigma_{2}^{\prime}}_{K_{2},\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}
is a normal algebraic space that is proper and flat over
\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}
and is representable by a projective scheme if
\Sigma
is projective.
Define
\mathcal{Z}_{[(\Phi_{2},\sigma_{2})],K_{2},\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}
to be the normalization of the normalized base change
\mathcal{Z}_{[ZP^{b}(\Phi,\sigma)],K,\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}
in
\mathrm{Z}_{[(\Phi_{2},\sigma_{2})],K_{2},E^{\prime}_{K_{Z}}}
via the composition of morphisms
\mathrm{Z}_{[(\Phi_{2},\sigma_{2})],K_{2},E^{\prime}_{K_{Z}}}\to\mathrm{Z}_{[ZP^{a}(\Phi,\sigma)],K,E^{\prime}_{K_{Z}}}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathrm{Z}_{[ZP^{b}(\Phi,\sigma)],K,E^{\prime}_{K_{Z}}}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{Z}_{[ZP^{b}(\Phi,\sigma)],K,\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}
. The middle isomorphism is also obtained from Corollary
1.44
. Let
\mathcal{Z}_{[ZP^{a}(\Phi,\sigma)],K,\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}
be the normalization of
\mathcal{Z}_{[ZP^{b}(\Phi,\sigma)],K,\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}
in
\mathrm{Z}_{[ZP^{a}(\Phi,\sigma)],K,\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}
, which is isomorphic to
\mathcal{Z}_{[ZP^{b}(\Phi,\sigma)],K,\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}
.

Convention 4.37
.

Let
X
be a normal algebraic space over
\mathcal{O}_{(v)}^{\prime}
. The symbol
X_{\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}
always denotes the normalized base change of
X
from
\mathcal{O}_{(v)}^{\prime}
to
\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}
; these are the usual base changes if we can choose
F_{K_{Z}}
such that
E^{\prime}_{K_{Z}}
is unramified over
E^{\prime}
at
p
.

Lemma 4.38
.

With the construction above, if
\Phi_{2}\xrightarrow[\sim]{(\gamma,q)_{K_{2}}}\Phi_{2}^{\prime}
for
\Phi_{2},\Phi_{2}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{CLR}(G_{2},X_{2})
,
\gamma\in G_{2}(\mathbb{Q})
and
q\in G_{2}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
, we obtain an isomorphism
\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{2}}}(P_{\Phi_{2}},D_{\Phi_{2}})\to\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{2}}^{\prime}}(P_{\Phi_{2}^{\prime}},D_{\Phi_{2}^{\prime}})
extending the one over the generic fiber (see
[
50
, 2.1.15]
). In particular,
\Delta_{\Phi_{2},K_{2}}
acts equivariantly on the tower
\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{2}}}\to\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{K_{\Phi_{2}}}\to\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{2}},h}
, and the action of
\Delta_{\Phi_{2},K_{2}}^{\circ}
factors through a finite quotient.

Proof.

Combine Proposition
4.24
, the construction above, and the following commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Phi_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Phi_{2}^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi^{a}_{*}(\Phi_{2})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi^{a}_{*}(\Phi_{2}^{\prime}).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\gamma,q)_{K_{2}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi^{a}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi^{a}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\gamma,q)_{K}}

The last claim is true since one can check this over the generic fiber and
\Delta^{\circ}_{\Phi_{2},K_{2}}
acts on the tower
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi_{2}}}\to\overline{\mathrm{Sh}}_{K_{\Phi_{2}}}\to\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi_{2}},h}
through a finite group.
∎

Let us state the main theorem of this paper.

Theorem 4.39
.

The triple
(G_{2},X_{2},K_{2,p})
can be regarded as one of the Cases (HS), (
\text{STB}_{n}
) and (DL) in §
4.2
by fixing some
K_{2,p}^{\prime}
containing
K_{2,p}
, where
K_{2,p}^{\prime}
is a hyperspecial subgroup or an intersection of
n
Bruhat-Tits stabilizer subgroups containing
K_{2,p}
as in Construction
4.36
. Let
K_{2}^{p}
be a neat open compact subgroup of
G_{2}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
and write
K_{2}:=K_{2,p}K_{2}^{p}
. Let
\Sigma_{2}
be an admissible cone decomposition of
(G_{2},X_{2},K_{2})
. There is an admissible cone decomposition
\Sigma^{\prime}_{2}
refining
\Sigma_{2}
, which can be chosen to be smooth, projective, or both smooth and projective. Let
\mathcal{O}_{2}
be
\mathcal{O}_{E_{2},(v_{2})}
in Case (HS) and its deep-level case, and let
\mathcal{O}_{2}
be
\mathcal{O}_{E_{2},v_{2}}
in Case (
\text{STB}_{n}
) and its deep-level case. With the constructions above, we have the following statements:

(
4.39
.1)

There is an integral model
\mathcal{S}_{K_{2}}^{\Sigma_{2}^{\prime}}
over
\mathcal{O}_{2}
extending

\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{2}}^{\Sigma_{2}^{\prime}}

which is a normal algebraic space that is proper and flat over
\mathcal{O}_{2}
. For a suitable refinement
\Sigma_{2}^{\prime}
,
\mathcal{S}_{K_{2}}^{\Sigma_{2}^{\prime}}
is representable by a normal scheme that is projective over
\mathcal{O}_{2}
.

(
4.39
.2)

The compactification
\mathcal{S}_{K_{2}}^{\Sigma_{2}^{\prime}}
has a good stratification

\mathcal{S}^{\Sigma_{2}^{\prime}}_{K_{2}}=\coprod\limits_{\Upsilon_{2}\in\mathrm{Cusp}_{K_{2}}(G_{2},X_{2},\Sigma_{2}^{\prime})}\mathcal{Z}_{\Upsilon_{2},K_{2}}

such that each stratum
\mathcal{Z}_{\Upsilon_{2},K_{2}}
is a locally closed normal subscheme labeled by an
\Upsilon_{2}=[(\Phi_{2},\sigma_{2})]
, which is flat over
\mathcal{O}_{2}
. Recall that there is a partial order “
\preceq
” on
\mathrm{Cusp}_{K_{2}}(G_{2},X_{2},\Sigma^{\prime}_{2})
(see
[
50
, 2.1.18]
and §
1.3.3
). The closure
\overline{\mathcal{Z}}_{\Upsilon_{2},K_{2}}
of

\mathcal{Z}_{\Upsilon_{2},K_{2}}
in
\mathcal{S}^{\Sigma_{2}^{\prime}}_{K_{2}}
satisfies
\overline{\mathcal{Z}}_{\Upsilon_{2},K_{2}}=\coprod\limits_{\Upsilon_{2}^{\prime}\preceq\Upsilon_{2}}\mathcal{Z}_{\Upsilon_{2}^{\prime},K_{2}}
. In particular, there is a natural open embedding
J^{\Sigma_{2}^{\prime}}:\mathcal{S}_{K_{2}}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{S}_{K_{2}}^{\Sigma_{2}^{\prime}}
making
\mathcal{S}_{K_{2}}
an open dense subscheme of
\mathcal{S}_{K_{2}}^{\Sigma_{2}^{\prime}}
.

(
4.39
.3)

For each cusp label representative
\Phi_{2}
of
(G_{2},X_{2})
, the scheme
\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{2}}}
is a normal scheme that is flat over
\mathcal{O}_{2}
whose generic fiber is
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi_{2}},\mathcal{O}_{2}\otimes\mathbb{Q}}
. There is a tower of normal
\mathcal{O}_{2}
-schemes

(4.22)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{2}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{K_{\Phi_{2}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{2}},h}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{p}_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{p}_{2}}

The first morphism
\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{2}}}\to\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{K_{\Phi_{2}}}
represents an
\mathbf{E}_{K_{\Phi_{2}}}
-torsor and the second morphism is proper.
In particular, since
\mathbf{p}_{1}
is an
\mathbf{E}_{K_{\Phi_{2}}}
-torsor, we can construct
\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{2}}}(\sigma_{2})
as the twisted affine torus embedding of
\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{2}}}
over
\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{K_{\Phi_{2}}}
with respect to the affine torus embedding
\mathbf{E}_{K_{\Phi_{2}}}\hookrightarrow\mathbf{E}_{K_{\Phi_{2}}}(\sigma_{2})
, and we can construct
\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{2}},\sigma_{2}}
as the
\sigma_{2}
-stratum of
\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{2}}}(\sigma_{2})
. Also, it makes sense to define
\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{2}}}(\Sigma^{\prime}_{2})
.

Moreover, let
\mathcal{S}^{*}_{K_{\Phi_{2}}}
(resp.
\overline{\mathcal{S}}^{*}_{K_{\Phi_{2}}}
and resp.
\mathcal{S}^{*}_{K_{\Phi_{2}},h}
) be the quotient of
\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{2}}}
(resp.
\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{K_{\Phi_{2}}}
and resp.
\mathcal{S}^{*}_{K_{\Phi_{2}},h}
) by
\Delta^{\circ}_{\Phi_{2},K_{2}}
. Then
\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{2}}}^{*}\to\overline{\mathcal{S}}^{*}_{K_{\Phi_{2}}}
is also a torsor under a split torus.

(
4.39
.4)

For any cusp label representative
\Phi_{2}
of
(G_{2},X_{2})
, the group
\Delta^{\circ}_{\Phi_{2},K_{2}}
acts on
\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{2}},\sigma_{2}}
and
\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi}}(\sigma_{2})
through a finite quotient. We have an isomorphism
\Delta_{\Phi_{2},K_{2}}^{\circ}\backslash\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{2}},\sigma_{2}}\cong\mathcal{Z}_{[(\Phi_{2},\sigma_{2})],K_{2}}
, where
\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{2}},\sigma_{2}}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{2}}}(\sigma_{2})
is the closed stratum of
\sigma_{2}
. The scheme
\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{2}}}(\sigma_{2})
has a natural stratification defined by the cone decomposition
\Sigma_{2}^{\prime}(\Phi_{2})
.

We have a strata-preserving isomorphism

\Delta^{\circ}_{{\Phi_{2}},K_{2}}\backslash{(\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{2}}}(\sigma_{2}))}{}^{\wedge{\ }}_{\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{2}},\sigma_{2}}}\cong{(\mathcal{S}^{\Sigma_{2}^{\prime}}_{K_{2}})}{}^{\wedge{\ }}_{\mathcal{Z}_{[(\Phi_{2},\sigma_{2})],K_{2}}}.

Moreover, let
\mathcal{S}^{*}_{K_{\Phi_{2}}}(\sigma_{2})
(resp.
\mathcal{S}^{*}_{K_{\Phi_{2}},\sigma_{2}}
) be the quotient of
\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{2}}}(\sigma_{2})
(resp.
\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{2},\sigma_{2}}}
) by
\Delta^{\circ}_{\Phi_{2},K_{2}}
. We have a strata-preserving isomorphism

\Delta^{\circ}_{{\Phi_{2}},K_{2}}\backslash{(\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{2}}}(\sigma_{2}))}{}^{\wedge{\ }}_{\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{2}},\sigma_{2}}}\cong{(\mathcal{S}^{\Sigma_{2}^{\prime}}_{K_{2}})}{}^{\wedge{\ }}_{\mathcal{Z}_{[(\Phi_{2},\sigma_{2})],K_{2}}}\cong{(\mathcal{S}^{*}_{K_{\Phi_{2}}}(\sigma_{2}))}{}^{\wedge{\ }}_{\mathcal{S}^{*}_{K_{\Phi_{2}},\sigma_{2}}}.

Here, “strata-preserving” has the following meaning: For any affine open formal subscheme
\spf(A,I)
of
{(\mathcal{S}^{\Sigma_{2}^{\prime}}_{K_{2}})}{}^{\wedge{\ }}_{\mathcal{Z}_{[(\Phi_{2},\sigma_{2})],K_{2}}}
which canonically induces morphisms
\mathsf{c}_{1}:\spec A\to\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{2}}}^{*}(\sigma_{2})
and
\mathsf{c}_{2}:\spec A\to\mathcal{S}^{\Sigma_{2}^{\prime}}_{K_{2}}
, the two stratifications on
\spec A
defined by the pullback of the stratification on
\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{2}}}(\sigma_{2})
under
\mathsf{c}_{1}
and the pullback of the stratification on
\mathcal{S}_{K_{2}}^{\Sigma_{2}^{\prime}}
under
\mathsf{c}_{2}
coincide.

(
4.39
.5)

In Case (HS), each

\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{2},p}}:=\varprojlim_{K_{\Phi_{2}}^{p}\text{ neat open compact}}\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{2},p}K_{\Phi_{2}}^{p}}

is an inverse limit of schemes that are smooth over
\mathcal{O}_{2}
and has the extension property. In particular, the tower
\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{2},p}}
does not depend on the choices made at the beginning of §
4.3.1
.

Remark 4.40
.

•

Our construction works for any choice of
(G_{0},X_{0})
that satisfies Condition (3) in
[
33
, Lem. 4.6.22]
; therefore, if, for some
(G_{2},X_{2})
, one can choose a
(G_{0},X_{0})
such that
E_{0}\subset E_{2}
, then
E^{\prime}=E_{2}
, and
\mathcal{S}_{K_{2}}^{\Sigma_{2}^{\prime}}
and
\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{2}}}
in the theorem are automatically defined over
\spec\mathcal{O}_{E_{2},(v_{2})}
.

•

The group
\Delta^{\circ}_{\Phi_{2},K_{2}}
and the action of it on
\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{2}}}
are not trivial for a general abelian-type Shimura datum
(G_{2},X_{2})
and a general
K_{2}
. Our result
(.3)
shows that both
\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{2}}}
and
\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{2}}}^{*}
are torsors under split tori. Note that instead of
\{\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{2}}}(\sigma_{2})\}
,
\{\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{2}}}^{*}(\sigma_{2})\}
is the collection of affine toric schemes attached to the toroidal embedding
\mathcal{S}_{K_{2}}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{S}_{K_{2}}^{\Sigma_{2}^{\prime}}
in the sense of
[
30
, p.54, Def. 1]
.

•

One can also formulate and prove a refinement of
(.4)
similar to
[
40
, Prop. 2.1.3]
. Note that this refined assertion was used in
[
40
]
and
[
52
]
to study the well-positionedness of some subschemes on compactifications of integral models of PEL- and Hodge-type Shimura varieties.

Proof of Theorem
4.39
: first reductions.
It suffices to show all of the statements above over
\mathcal{O}^{\prime}_{(v)}
.
The statement
(.2)
will be shown along the way in the proof of

(.1)
and
(.4)
(see below).
For
(.3)
, it suffices to show that
\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{2}}}\to\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{K_{\Phi_{2}}}
and
\mathcal{S}^{*}_{K_{\Phi_{2}}}\to\overline{\mathcal{S}}^{*}_{K_{\Phi_{2}}}
are torsors under
\mathbf{E}_{K_{\Phi_{2}}}
and
\mathbf{E}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi_{2}}}
, respectively; other claims in it follow from Construction
4.35
,
4.36
and Lemma
4.38
.

\square

4.3.2.
Proof of
(.3)
.

§
4.3.2
and §
4.3.3
are devoted to the proof of
(.3)
.

First, we need the following preparation.

Lemma 4.41

(cf.
[
50
, Lem. 3.7.2]
and
[
45
, Prop. 8.7]
)
.

Let
K_{0,p}
be an open compact subgroup contained in some intersection
K_{0,p}^{\prime}\subset G_{0}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
of

n
Bruhat-Tits stabilizer subgroups. Let
K^{p}_{0}
be a neat open compact subgroup in
G_{0}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
. Let
K_{0}:=K_{0,p}K_{0}^{p}
and
K_{0}^{\prime}:=K_{0,p}^{\prime}K_{0}^{p}
.
Let
\Phi_{0}
be a cusp label representative of
(G_{0},X_{0})
mapping to
\Phi^{\ddagger}\in\mathcal{CLR}(G^{\ddagger},X^{\ddagger})
. Let
K_{\Phi_{0}}
(resp.
K_{\Phi_{0}}^{\prime}
) be the intersection
{}^{g_{\Phi_{0}}}{K_{0}}\cap P_{\Phi_{0}}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
(resp.
{}^{g_{\Phi_{0}}}{K^{\prime}_{0}}\cap P_{\Phi_{0}}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
). As before, suppose that
\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{0}}}
is constructed from the relative normalization of
\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{0}}^{\prime}}
in
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi_{0}}}
, and that
\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{0}}^{\prime}}
is constructed by choosing
K_{p}^{\ddagger}\subset G^{\ddagger}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
and a Hodge embedding as in (
\text{STB}_{n}
), and by taking the relative normalization of
\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi^{\ddagger}}}
in
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi_{0}}^{\prime}}
. Then there is a finite flat cover
U
of
\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{K_{\Phi_{0}}^{\prime}}
and an
\mathbf{E}_{K_{\Phi_{0}}}
-torsor
\mathcal{S}
over
U
, such that the pullback of the torsor
\mathcal{S}\to U
to
U^{\prime}:=U\times_{\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{K^{\prime}_{\Phi_{0}}}}\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{K_{\Phi_{0}}}
is isomorphic to the pullback of the torsor
\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{0}}}\to\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{K_{\Phi_{0}}}
to the finite flat cover
U^{\prime}
of the target.

Proof.

We combine
[
50
, Lem. 3.7.2]
with
[
50
, Thm. 4.1.5]
.
Choose some neat open compact
K^{{\ddagger},\prime}:=K_{p}^{{\ddagger},\prime}K^{{\ddagger},\prime,p}\subset K^{\ddagger}
containing
K_{0}
such that
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi_{0}}}\to\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi^{\ddagger}}^{\prime}}
is a closed embedding, where
K_{\Phi^{\ddagger}}^{\prime}:=P_{\Phi^{\ddagger}}(\mathbb{A}_{f})\cap{}^{g_{\Phi^{\ddagger}}}{K^{{\ddagger},\prime}}
. This can be achieved by Corollary
1.10
and Lemma
1.22
.
Then
\mathbf{E}_{K_{\Phi_{0}}}\to\mathbf{E}_{K_{\Phi^{\ddagger}}^{\prime}}
is a closed embedding. By
[
50
, Thm. 4.1.5(4)]
,
\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{0}}}\to\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi^{\ddagger}}^{\prime}}
is equivariant under
\mathbf{E}_{K_{\Phi_{0}}}\to\mathbf{E}_{K_{\Phi^{\ddagger}}^{\prime}}
.
By the proof of
[
50
, Lem. 3.7.2]
, there is a finite flat cover
U^{\ddagger}
of
\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{K_{\Phi^{\ddagger}}}
, a split torus
\mathbf{E}
and an
\mathbf{E}
-torsor
\mathcal{S}^{\ddagger}
over
U^{\ddagger}
with an isogeny between split tori
\mathbf{E}\to\mathbf{E}_{K_{\Phi^{\ddagger}}^{\prime}}
, such that the pullback of
\mathcal{S}^{\ddagger}\times^{\mathbf{E}}\mathbf{E}_{K^{\prime}_{\Phi^{\ddagger}}}\to U^{\ddagger}
to
U^{{\ddagger},\prime}:=\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{K^{\prime}_{\Phi^{\ddagger}}}\times_{\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{K_{\Phi^{\ddagger}}}}U^{\ddagger}
is isomorphic to the pullback of
\mathcal{S}_{K^{\prime}_{\Phi^{\ddagger}}}\to\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{K^{\prime}_{\Phi^{\ddagger}}}
to
U^{{\ddagger},\prime}
.
There is a projection
\mathbf{S}_{K^{\prime}_{\Phi^{\ddagger}}}\to\mathbf{S}_{K_{\Phi_{0}}}
between character groups associated with
\mathbf{E}_{K^{\prime}_{\Phi^{\ddagger}}}
and
\mathbf{E}_{K_{\Phi_{0}}}
.

Let

U:=U^{\ddagger}\times_{\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{K_{\Phi^{\ddagger}}}}\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{K^{\prime}_{\Phi_{0}}},

and let

\mathcal{S}^{\ddagger}_{1}:=\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi^{\ddagger}}}\times_{\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{K_{\Phi^{\ddagger}}}}\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{K^{\prime}_{\Phi_{0}}}.

Moreover, let
\mathcal{S}_{0}^{\ddagger}
be the pullback of the torsor
\mathcal{S}^{\ddagger}\times^{\mathbf{E}}\mathbf{E}_{K^{\prime}_{\Phi^{\ddagger}}}
over
U^{\ddagger}
along
U\to U^{\ddagger}
.

Then there is a diagram of torsors

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{S}_{0}^{\ddagger}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{S}_{K^{\prime}_{\Phi_{0}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{S}^{\ddagger}_{1},}

where the bottom one is equivariant under
\mathbf{E}_{K_{\Phi_{0}}^{\prime}}\to\mathbf{E}_{K_{\Phi^{\ddagger}}}
over
\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{K^{\prime}_{\Phi_{0}}}
(and the last map between tori can be chosen as an embedding by Corollary
1.10
and Lemma
1.22
again), and the vertical arrow is equivariant under
\mathbf{E}_{K^{\prime}_{\Phi^{\ddagger}}}\to\mathbf{E}_{K_{\Phi^{\ddagger}}}
.

We now take the inverse image of the
\mathbf{E}_{K^{\prime}_{\Phi_{0}}}
-torsor
\mathcal{S}_{K^{\prime}_{\Phi_{0}}}
in the
\mathbf{E}_{K^{\prime}_{\Phi^{\ddagger}}}
-torsor
\mathcal{S}_{0}^{\ddagger}
by pulling back under the vertical map in the displayed diagram. This is an
\mathbf{E}_{K_{\Phi_{0}}}
-torsor
\mathcal{S}
over
U
.

Then the torsor
\mathcal{S}\to U
is the desired one, as
\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{0}}}
is an
\mathbf{E}_{K_{\Phi_{0}}}
-torsor by
[
50
, Thm. 4.1.5]
and has an
\mathbf{E}_{K_{\Phi_{0}}}
-equivariant morphism to
\mathcal{S}
by checking using the universal property of fiber products in the construction.
∎

Remark 4.42
.

If there is another open compact subgroup
K_{0,p}^{\prime\prime}\subset G_{0}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
such that
K_{0,p}\subset K_{0,p}^{\prime\prime}\subset K_{0,p}^{\prime}
. Let
K^{\prime\prime}:=K_{0,p}^{\prime\prime}K^{p}_{0}
and
K^{\prime\prime}_{\Phi_{0}}:={}^{g_{\Phi_{0}}}{K_{0}^{\prime\prime}}\cap P_{\Phi_{0}}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
. Then we can pull
U
back to
U^{\prime\prime}:=\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{K_{\Phi_{0}}^{\prime\prime}}(P_{\Phi_{0}},D_{\Phi_{0}})\times_{\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{K^{\prime}_{\Phi_{0}}}}U
and also pull the torsor
\mathcal{S}
over
U
back to
U^{\prime\prime}
. We denote this pullback of
\mathcal{S}
by
\mathcal{S}^{\prime\prime}
. Then Lemma
4.41
above implies that the pullback of
\mathcal{S}^{\prime\prime}
to
U^{\prime}
is isomorphic to the pullback of
\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{0}}}\to\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{K_{\Phi_{0}}}
to the finite flat cover
U^{\prime}
.

4.3.3.

Let us show the following lemma. The proof of it occupies all paragraphs below until Lemma
4.48
.

Lemma 4.43
.

The morphism
\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}^{a}}\to\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}^{a}}
is an
\mathbf{E}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}}
-torsor. The assertion in
(.3)
that
\mathcal{S}^{*}_{K_{\Phi_{2}}}\to\overline{\mathcal{S}}^{*}_{K_{\Phi_{2}}}
is a torsor under a split torus is true.

By Proposition
1.35
, we see that it suffices to show the statement for
\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}^{a}_{\Phi}}\to\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{\widetilde{K}^{a}_{\Phi}}
since
\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{2}}}^{*}\cong\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{K_{\Phi_{2}}}^{*}\times_{\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{\widetilde{K}^{a}_{\Phi}}}\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}^{a}_{\Phi}}
by our choice of
K_{p}K^{p}
satisfying the proposition.

Recall that we have already shown that
\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}^{b}_{\Phi}}\to\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{\widetilde{K}^{b}_{\Phi}}
is an
\mathbf{E}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}}
-torsor for all cases in Part
1
of Proposition
4.30
. As in Construction
4.35
, we have proved that
\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}^{a}_{\Phi}}\to\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{\widetilde{K}^{a}_{\Phi}}
is an
\mathbf{E}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}}
-torsor, when we can choose
F_{Z}
such that
\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}
is unramified over
\mathcal{O}^{\prime}_{(v)}
. This is the case when
K_{p}
contains the parahoric subgroup of
Z(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
. For general
K_{p}
, we know from Construction
4.36
that
\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}^{a}_{\Phi},\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}\to\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{\widetilde{K}^{a}_{\Phi},\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}
is an
\mathbf{E}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}}
-torsor.

Recall that we fixed some intersection of Bruhat-Tits stabilizer subgroups
K_{p}^{\prime}
containing
K_{p}
.

Denote
K^{\prime}:=K_{p}^{\prime}K^{p}
. Let
K_{Z,p}^{+}\subset Z_{G}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
be the unique parahoric subgroup of
Z_{G}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
. Denote
K_{p}^{+}:=K_{p}K_{Z,p}^{+}
and
K^{+}:=K_{p}^{+}K^{p}
. Let
\widetilde{K}^{+}_{\Phi}:=ZP_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f})\cap g_{\Phi}K^{+}g_{\Phi}^{-1}
. Note that
\widetilde{K}^{+}_{\Phi}
contains the parahoric subgroup of
Z_{G}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
.
Then the scheme
\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}^{+,i}_{\Phi}}:=\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}^{+}_{\Phi}}(ZP^{i}_{\Phi},ZP^{i}_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})D_{\Phi})
obtained as in Construction
4.36
is a torsor over
\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{\widetilde{K}^{+,i}_{\Phi}}:=\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{\widetilde{K}^{+}_{\Phi}}(ZP^{i}_{\Phi},ZP^{i}_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})D_{\Phi})
under
\mathbf{E}_{\widetilde{K}^{+}_{\Phi}}
.
Note that
\mathbf{E}_{\widetilde{K}^{+}_{\Phi}}
is determined by the lattice
\mathbf{\Lambda}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}^{+}}
, the projection to the second factor of
(Z_{G}(\mathbb{Q})\times U_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q}))\cap\widetilde{K}^{+}_{\Phi}
, and there is an isogeny
\mathbf{E}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}}\to\mathbf{E}_{\widetilde{K}^{+}_{\Phi}}
.
We then have a finite map
\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}^{a}}\to\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}^{a}}\times_{\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{\widetilde{K}^{+}_{\Phi}}}\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}^{+}_{\Phi}}
that is equivariant under
\mathbf{E}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}}\to\mathbf{E}_{\widetilde{K}^{+}_{\Phi}}
.

Recall that by construction we have a finite index
I_{ZP_{\Phi}/P_{0},K}
mapping to another finite index
I_{ZP_{\Phi}/P_{0},K^{+}}
. Without loss of generality, we pick some
e_{\beta}
for
\beta\in I_{ZP_{\Phi}/P_{0},K}
mapping to
\beta_{1}\in I_{ZP_{\Phi}/P_{0},K^{+}}
, and let
e_{\beta}=e_{\beta_{1}}
.

Denote
\Delta^{\beta}:=\Delta_{{K}^{\beta}}(P_{0},ZP_{\Phi})
and
\Delta^{\beta_{1}}:=\Delta_{{K^{\prime,\beta_{1}}}}(P_{0},ZP_{\Phi})
.
The quotient
\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}^{b}_{\Phi},\beta}:=\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{\beta^{\prime}}}}/\Delta^{\beta}
(resp.
\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{\widetilde{K}^{b}_{\Phi},\beta}:=\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{K_{\Phi_{\beta^{\prime}}}}/\Delta^{\beta}
) is an open and closed subscheme of
\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}^{b}_{\Phi}}
(resp.
\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{\widetilde{K}^{b}_{\Phi}}
).
Similarly, the quotient
\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}^{+,b}_{\Phi},\beta_{1}}:=\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{\beta_{1}^{\prime}}}}/\Delta^{\beta_{1}}
(resp.
\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{\widetilde{K}^{+,b}_{\Phi},\beta_{1}}:=\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{K_{\Phi_{\beta_{1}^{\prime}}}}/\Delta^{\beta_{1}}
) is an open and closed subscheme of
\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}^{+,b}}
.
Then
\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}^{b},\beta}
(resp.
\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}^{b},\beta}
) maps surjectively to
\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}^{+,b},\beta_{1}}
(resp.
\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}^{+,b},\beta_{1}}
).

The action of
\Delta^{\beta_{1}}
on
\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{K_{\Phi_{\beta_{1}^{\prime}}}}
factors through a finite quotient
\overline{H}_{\beta_{1}}
, and the action of the latter group is free by Lemma
4.25
; denote by
\mathcal{K}_{\beta_{1}}
the kernel of this action. Let
\Delta_{1}:=\Delta^{\beta}\cap\mathcal{K}_{\beta_{1}}
. Let
\Delta^{1}:=\Delta^{\beta}/\Delta_{1}
.
Define
\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{1}:=\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{K_{\Phi_{\beta^{\prime}}}}/\Delta_{1}
. Define
\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{2}:=\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{K_{\Phi_{\beta_{1}^{\prime}}}}/\Delta^{1}.

Lemma 4.44
.

There is a Cartesian commutative diagram

(4.23)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{K_{\Phi_{\beta_{1}^{\prime}}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{S}_{\widetilde{K}^{b}_{\Phi},\beta}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{2},}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{2}}

which fits into a commutative diagram

(4.24)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{K_{\Phi_{\beta^{\prime}}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{K_{\Phi_{\beta_{1}^{\prime}}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{\widetilde{K}^{b}_{\Phi},\beta}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}^{+,b},\beta_{1}}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{can.}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi^{b}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi^{b}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{can.}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h_{2}}

The compositions of horizontal arrows of the diagram above are canonical transition maps induced by relative normalizations.

Proof.

The compositions of finite surjective morphisms between normal schemes
\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{K_{\Phi_{\beta^{\prime}}}}\to\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{1}\xrightarrow{g_{1}}\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{\widetilde{K}^{b}_{\Phi},\beta}
and
\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{K_{\Phi_{\beta_{1}^{\prime}}}}\xrightarrow{g_{2}}\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{2}\to\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{K^{+,b}_{\Phi},\beta_{1}}
are finite étale. So
g_{1}
and
g_{2}
are finite étale. Moreover, (
4.23
) is Cartesian since
g_{1}
and
g_{2}
are locally finite free of the same degree by Lemma
4.25
.
∎

By construction,
\Delta^{\beta_{1}}
is generated by
\Delta^{\beta}
and
\Delta^{+}:=\ker(\mathscr{A}(P_{0})\to\mathscr{A}(ZP_{\Phi})/K_{Z,p}^{+})
, and both
\Delta^{\beta}
and
\Delta^{+}
are normal subgroups of
\Delta^{\beta_{1}}
;
\Delta^{+}
is finite modulo
K_{\Phi_{\beta_{1}^{\prime}}}\cap\Delta^{+}
and satisfies the assumption in §
3.4.2
(that
h\gamma^{-1}
is trivial in
G^{\mathrm{ad}}_{0}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
).

By Lemma
4.41
and Remark
4.42
above and by taking contracted products of
\mathbf{E}_{K_{\Phi_{\beta^{\prime}}}}
-torsors along
\mathbf{E}_{K_{\Phi_{\beta^{\prime}}}}\to\mathbf{E}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}}
, there is a finite flat cover
V(\beta_{1})
of
\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi_{\beta_{1}^{\prime}}}}
and an
\mathbf{E}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}}
-torsor
\mathcal{T}(\beta_{1})
over
V(\beta_{1})
, such that the pullback of the torsor
\mathcal{T}(\beta_{1})\to V(\beta_{1})
to
V^{\prime}(\beta):=\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{K_{\Phi_{\beta^{\prime}}}}\times_{\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{K_{\Phi_{\beta_{1}^{\prime}}}}}V(\beta_{1})
is isomorphic to the pullback of
\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{\beta^{\prime}}}}\times^{\mathbf{E}_{K_{\Phi_{\beta^{\prime}}}}}\mathbf{E}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}}
over
\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{K_{\Phi_{\beta^{\prime}}}}
to
V^{\prime}(\beta)
.

Let
V(\beta):=V(\beta_{1})\times_{\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{K_{\Phi_{\beta_{1}^{\prime}}}},f_{1}}\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{1}
.
Taking the quotient of
\Delta_{1}
and by Lemma
4.44
above, we have that
the pullback of
\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}^{b}_{\Phi},\beta}\to\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{\widetilde{K}^{b}_{\Phi},\beta}
along the finite flat cover
V(\beta)\to\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{1}\xrightarrow{g_{1}}\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{\widetilde{K}^{b}_{\Phi},\beta}
is isomorphic to the pullback of
\mathcal{T}(\beta_{1})\to V(\beta_{1})
to
V(\beta)
.
Moreover, we have
V(\beta)\times_{V(\beta_{1})}\mathcal{T}(\beta_{1})\cong\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{\widetilde{K}^{b}_{\Phi},\beta}\times_{f_{2},\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{2}}\mathcal{T}(\beta_{1})
by Lemma
4.44
. (The morphism from
\mathcal{T}(\beta_{1})
to
\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{2}
is given by
\mathcal{T}(\beta_{1})\to V(\beta_{1})\to\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{K_{\Phi_{\beta_{1}^{\prime}}}}\xrightarrow{g_{2}}\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{2}
.)

In conclusion, we have a canonical isomorphism

\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}^{b}_{\Phi},\beta}\times_{\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{\widetilde{K}^{b}_{\Phi},\beta}}V(\beta)\cong\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{\widetilde{K}^{b}_{\Phi},\beta}\times_{f_{2},\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{2}}\mathcal{T}(\beta_{1}).

This isomorphism is defined over
\mathcal{O}^{\prime}_{(v)}
.

Lemma 4.45
.

With the conventions above, there is a finite flat cover
V(\beta_{1})
over
\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi_{\beta_{1}^{\prime}}}}
and an
\mathbf{E}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}}
-torsor
\mathcal{T}(\beta_{1})
over
V(\beta_{1})
such that the sequence
\mathcal{T}(\beta_{1})\to V(\beta_{1})\to\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi_{\beta_{1}^{\prime}}}}
is equivariant under the action of
\Delta^{+}
.

Proof.

Since
\Delta^{+}\subset\Delta^{\beta_{1}}
satisfies the assumption in §
3.4.2
and is finite modulo
K_{\Phi_{\beta_{1}^{\prime}}}\cap\Delta^{+}
, we can fix a sufficiently large finite Galois extension
F
over
\mathbb{Q}
, and replace
(G^{\ddagger},X^{\ddagger})
in Lemma
4.41
with
(G^{F},X^{F})
. In the notation of §
3.4.2
,
\Delta^{+}
acts on the integral models
\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi^{F}}}\to\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{K_{\Phi^{F}}}\to\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi^{F}},h}
associated with the cusp label representative
\Phi^{F}
of
(G^{F},X^{F})
and the universal
1
-motive on
\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi^{F}}}
.
We now adopt the notation in Lemma
4.41
. Suppose
\Phi_{\beta_{1}^{\prime}}
maps to
\Phi^{F}
. By
[
50
, Lem. 3.7.2]
, the finite flat cover
U^{\ddagger}
over
\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi^{F}}}
is
\mathrm{Hom}(\frac{1}{n}Y_{\mathcal{Q}_{\Phi^{F}}},A_{\mathcal{Q}_{\Phi^{F}}})
, where
n
is some positive integer and
Y_{\mathcal{Q}_{\Phi^{F}}}
(resp.
A_{Q_{\Phi^{F}}}
) is the
(-2)
-graded piece (resp.
(-1)
-graded piece) of the universal
1
-motive
\mathcal{Q}_{\Phi^{F}}
over
\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi^{F}}}
, and the torsor
\mathcal{S}_{0}^{\ddagger}
is a quotient of
\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}^{\mathrm{symm}}(\frac{1}{n}Y_{\mathcal{Q}_{\Phi^{F}}},\mathcal{G}^{\natural}_{\mathcal{Q}_{\Phi^{F}}})
(see
[
50
, 3.7.2.2]
).
Then, by Lemma
3.27
, the sequence
\mathcal{S}_{0}^{\ddagger}\times^{\mathbf{E}_{K_{\Phi_{0}}}}\mathbf{E}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}}\to U^{\ddagger}\to\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{K_{\Phi^{F}}}
is equivariant under the action of
\Delta^{+}
after pulling back to
\mathcal{S}_{\Phi_{\beta_{1}^{\prime}}}
. We now have the desired assertion.
∎

Now we can and we will assume that
\mathcal{T}(\beta_{1})\to V(\beta_{1})
satisfies both Lemma
4.45
and Remark
4.42
.

Lemma 4.46
.

With the conventions above, the pullback of
V(\beta_{1})\to\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{K_{\Phi_{\beta_{1}^{\prime}}}}\xrightarrow{\pi^{b}}\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{\widetilde{K}^{+,b}_{\Phi},\beta_{1}}
along the transition map
\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{\widetilde{K}^{b}_{\Phi},\beta}\to\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}^{+,b},\beta_{1}}
is a finite flat cover
U(\beta)
of
\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{\widetilde{K}^{b}_{\Phi},\beta}
. The pullback of
\mathcal{T}(\beta_{1})
to
V_{\beta}
is canonically isomorphic to the pullback of
\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}^{b}_{\Phi},\beta}
to
U(\beta)
.

Proof.

The first claim is immediate from construction.
The diagram (
4.24
) is equivariant under any
\delta\in\Delta^{+}
. Combining this with Lemma
4.45
, we have the following commutative diagram:

(4.25)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{T}(\beta_{1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V(\beta_{1})}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{\widetilde{K}^{b}_{\Phi},\beta}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{2}}

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\delta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{2}}

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi^{b}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\delta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\delta}

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi^{b}}

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi^{b}}

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\delta}

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\delta}

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h_{2}}

From now on, we will freely use the conventions appearing in (
4.25
).
The quotient
\Delta_{0}:=\Delta^{\beta_{1}}/\Delta^{\beta}\cdot\mathcal{K}_{\beta_{1}}
acts on
\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{2}
freely. Since
\Delta^{+}
and
\Delta^{\beta}
generate
\Delta^{\beta_{1}}
and are normal subgroups,
\Delta_{0}
is a quotient of
\Delta^{+}
. Hence, the fiber product
\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}^{b},\beta}\times_{\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}^{+,b},\beta_{1}}}\mathcal{T}(\beta_{1})
is isomorphic to a disjoint union

\coprod_{[\delta]\in\Delta_{0}}\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{\widetilde{K}^{b}_{\Phi},\beta}\times_{f_{2},\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{2},[\delta]\circ g_{2}\circ\pi_{2}\circ\pi_{1}}\mathcal{T}(\beta_{1}).

Similarly, the fiber product
U(\beta)=\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}^{b},\beta}\times_{\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}^{+,b},\beta_{1}}}V(\beta_{1})
is isomorphic to a disjoint union

\coprod_{[\delta]\in\Delta_{0}}\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{\widetilde{K}^{b}_{\Phi},\beta}\times_{f_{2},\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{2},[\delta]\circ g_{2}\circ\pi_{2}}V(\beta_{1}).

From (
4.25
) and Lemma
4.44
, we see that
\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}^{b},\beta}\times_{\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}^{+,b},\beta_{1}}}\mathcal{T}(\beta_{1})
is isomorphic to a disjoint union

\coprod_{\{\delta\}}\delta^{*}(\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{\widetilde{K}^{b}_{\Phi},\beta}\times_{f_{2},\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{2}}\mathcal{T}(\beta_{1}))\cong\coprod_{\{\delta\}}\delta^{*}(\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}^{b}_{\Phi},\beta}\times_{\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{\widetilde{K}^{b}_{\Phi},\beta}}V(\beta))\cong\coprod_{\{\delta\}}\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}^{b}_{\Phi},\beta}\times_{\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{\widetilde{K}^{b}_{\Phi},\beta}}\delta^{*}V(\beta),

for a complete set of representatives
\{\delta\}
in
\Delta^{+}
of the quotient group
\Delta^{\beta_{1}}/\Delta^{\beta}\cdot\mathcal{K}_{\beta_{1}}
. For a different choice of representatives, the left-hand side of the displayed equation above remains unchanged since the composition of the map from
\mathcal{T}(\beta_{1})
to
\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{2}
is always equal to
[\delta]\circ g_{2}\circ\pi_{2}\circ\pi_{1}
.
We continue to compute

\coprod_{\{\delta\}}\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}^{b}_{\Phi},\beta}\times_{\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{\widetilde{K}^{b}_{\Phi},\beta}}\delta^{*}V(\beta)\cong\coprod_{\{\delta\}}\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}^{b}_{\Phi},\beta}\times_{\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{\widetilde{K}^{b}_{\Phi},\beta},[\delta]\circ g_{1}\circ f_{1}^{*}\pi_{2}}V(\beta).

By Lemma
4.44
, the last expression is isomorphic to

\coprod_{[\delta]\in\Delta_{0}}\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}^{b}_{\Phi},\beta}\times_{f_{2}\circ\mathbf{p}_{1},\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{2},[\delta]\circ g_{2}\circ\pi_{2}}V(\beta_{1})\cong\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}^{b}_{\Phi},\beta}\times_{\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{\widetilde{K}^{b}_{\Phi},\beta}}U(\beta).

The second claim has been shown.
∎

Write
\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}^{b}_{\Phi}}
(resp.
\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{\widetilde{K}^{b}_{\Phi}}
) as a disjoint union

\coprod_{\beta\in I_{ZP_{\Phi}/P_{0},K}}\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}^{b}_{\Phi},\beta}
(resp.
\coprod_{\beta\in I_{ZP_{\Phi}/P_{0},K}}\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{\widetilde{K}^{b}_{\Phi},\beta}
). Let
\mathcal{U}:=\coprod_{\beta\in I_{ZP_{\Phi}/P_{0},K}}U(\beta)
,
\mathcal{V}:=\coprod_{\beta_{1}\in I_{ZP_{\Phi}/P_{0},K^{+}}}V(\beta_{1})
and
\mathcal{T}:=\coprod_{\beta_{1}\in I_{ZP_{\Phi}/P_{0},K^{+}}}\mathcal{T}(\beta_{1})
.

Lemma 4.47
.

From the constructions above, we have a finite flat cover
\mathcal{V}
over
\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{\widetilde{K}^{+,b}_{\Phi}}
and an
\mathbf{E}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}}
-torsor
\mathcal{T}
over
\mathcal{V}
, such that the pullback of
\mathcal{V}
along the transition map
\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}^{b}}\to\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{\widetilde{K}^{+,b}_{\Phi}}
is
\mathcal{U}
and such that there is an isomorphism of
\mathbf{E}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}}
-torsors

\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}^{b}_{\Phi}}\times_{\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{\widetilde{K}^{b}_{\Phi}}}\mathcal{U}\cong\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}^{b}}\times_{\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{\widetilde{K}^{+,b}_{\Phi}}}\mathcal{T}.

Proof.

Combine Lemma
4.46
with the definitions above. Note that for distinct elements
\beta
and
\beta^{*}
in
I_{ZP_{\Phi}/P_{0},K}
mapping to the same
\beta_{1}\in I_{ZP_{\Phi}/P_{0},K^{+}}
, both
U(\beta)
and
U(\beta^{*})
are the pullbacks of the same
V(\beta_{1})
by (
4.25
) again since
\beta^{*}\equiv\delta\beta
in
I_{ZP_{\Phi}/P_{0},K}
for some
\delta\in\Delta^{+}
lifting an element in
K_{Z,p}^{+}
.
∎

Proof of Lemma
4.43
.

By Lemma
4.47
, we have

(4.26)

\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}^{b}_{\Phi}}\times_{\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{\widetilde{K}^{b}_{\Phi}}}\mathcal{U}\cong\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}^{b}}\times_{\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{\widetilde{K}^{+,b}_{\Phi}}}\mathcal{T},

and this isomorphism is equivariant under
K_{Z,p}^{+}
-action by (
4.25
), where
K_{Z,p}^{+}
acts equivariantly on
\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}^{b}}\to\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}^{b}}
and trivially on
\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{\widetilde{K}^{+,b}_{\Phi}}
, and this induces
K_{Z,p}^{+}
-action on both sides of (
4.26
).

Let
\mathcal{U}^{*}:=\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{\widetilde{K}^{b}_{\Phi},\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}\times_{\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{\widetilde{K}^{b}_{\Phi}}}\mathcal{U}
. Pulling back (
4.26
) along
\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{\widetilde{K}^{b}_{\Phi},\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}\to\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{\widetilde{K}^{b}_{\Phi}}
, we have an isomorphism

\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}^{b}_{\Phi},\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}\times_{\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{\widetilde{K}^{b}_{\Phi},\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}}\mathcal{U}^{*}\cong\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{\widetilde{K}^{b}_{\Phi},\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}\times_{\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{\widetilde{K}^{+,b}_{\Phi}}}\mathcal{T}.

Let
E^{\prime,ur}_{K_{Z}}:=E^{\prime}_{K_{Z}}\cap E^{p}
and
\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}^{ur}:=\mathcal{O}_{E^{\prime,ur}_{K_{Z}}}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{E^{\prime}}}\mathcal{O}_{E^{\prime},(v)}
. The
\mathrm{Gal}(E^{\prime}_{K_{Z}}/E^{\prime})
-action on the left-hand side is given by the descent datum of
\mathrm{Sh}_{\widetilde{K}^{b}_{\Phi},E^{\prime}_{K_{Z}}}
from
E^{\prime}_{K_{Z}}
to
E^{\prime}
, and it differs from the descent datum of
\mathrm{Sh}_{\widetilde{K}^{a}_{\Phi},E^{\prime}_{K_{Z}}}
from
E^{\prime}_{K_{Z}}
to
E^{\prime}
by
r_{E^{\prime},K_{Z,p}}
, where
r_{E^{\prime},K_{Z,p}}
assigns to any
\sigma\in\mathrm{Gal}(E^{\prime}_{K_{Z}}/E^{\prime})
the multiplication of an element in
Z_{G}(\mathbb{Q})\backslash Z_{G}(\mathbb{A}_{f})/K_{Z,p}
on
\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}^{b}_{\Phi},\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}
.
Since
\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}^{b}_{\Phi},\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}\cong\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}^{a}_{\Phi},\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}
,
\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{\widetilde{K}^{b}_{\Phi},\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}\cong\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{\widetilde{K}^{a}_{\Phi},\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}
and
\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{\widetilde{K}^{+,b}_{\Phi},\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}^{ur}}\cong\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{\widetilde{K}^{+,a}_{\Phi},\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}^{ur}}
,
by taking the
\mathrm{Gal}(E^{\prime}_{K_{Z}}/E^{\prime,ur}_{K_{Z}})
-quotient of the right-hand side of

(4.27)

\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}^{a}_{\Phi},\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}\times_{\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{\widetilde{K}^{a}_{\Phi},\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}}\mathcal{U}^{*}\cong\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{\widetilde{K}^{a}_{\Phi},\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}\times_{\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{\widetilde{K}^{+,a}_{\Phi},\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}^{ur}}}\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}^{ur}}

we see
\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}^{a}_{\Phi},\mathcal{O}^{ur}_{K_{Z}}}\to\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{\widetilde{K}^{a}_{\Phi},\mathcal{O}^{ur}_{K_{Z}}}
is an fppf
\mathbf{E}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}}
-torsor. The desired result now follows since it has been shown that
\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}^{a}_{\Phi}}
is an fppf
\mathbf{E}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}}
-torsor and
\mathbf{E}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}}
is smooth.

\square

Lemma 4.48
.

The statement
(.3)
is true.

Proof.

It remains to show that
\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{2}}}\to\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{K_{\Phi_{2}}}
is an
\mathbf{E}_{K_{\Phi_{2}}}
-torsor.
For a fixed
K_{\Phi_{2}}=K_{\Phi_{2},p}K^{p}_{\Phi_{2}}
, there is a neat open compact subgroup
K^{p,\uparrow}_{\Phi}
of
ZP_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
such that
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi_{2}}}\to\mathrm{Sh}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi,p}K_{\Phi}^{p,\uparrow}}(ZP^{a}_{\Phi},ZP^{a}_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})D_{\Phi})
is an open and closed embedding by Lemma
1.22
and Proposition
1.35
. Moreover, we can choose a sufficiently small
K^{p}\subset G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
containing
g_{\Phi_{2}}^{-1}K_{\Phi}^{p,\uparrow}g_{\Phi_{2}}
such that
g_{\Phi_{2}}K^{p}g_{\Phi_{2}}^{-1}\cap ZP_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})=K_{\Phi}^{p,\uparrow}
. We can then deduce the desired result from the result for
\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}^{a}}
:
In fact, under this choice of
K^{p}
,
\mathrm{Sh}_{\widetilde{K}^{a}_{\Phi}}\to\overline{\mathrm{Sh}}_{\widetilde{K}^{a}_{\Phi}}
is an
\mathbf{E}_{K_{\Phi_{2}}}
-torsor since
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi_{2}}}\to\mathrm{Sh}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi,p}K_{\Phi}^{p,\uparrow}}(ZP^{a}_{\Phi},ZP^{a}_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})D_{\Phi})
is a closed embedding.
Then
\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{2}}}\cong\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{K_{\Phi_{2}}}\times_{\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{\widetilde{K}^{a}_{\Phi}}}\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}^{a}}
by Zariski’s main theorem.
∎

4.3.4.
End of the proof.

Lemma 4.49
.

The statements
(.1)
,
(.2)
and
(.4)
are true.

Proof.

From Proposition
2.48
, exactly the same proof as Proposition
4.32
and Construction
4.36
, we have that

\mathcal{Z}_{[ZP^{a}({\Phi},\sigma)],K,\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}\cong\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}^{a}_{\Phi},\sigma,\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}

and that

(4.28)

{(\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}^{a}}(\sigma)_{\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}})}{}^{\wedge{\ }}_{\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}^{a},\sigma,\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}}\cong{(\mathcal{S}^{\Sigma}_{K,\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}})}{}^{\wedge{\ }}_{\mathcal{Z}_{[ZP^{a}(\Phi,\sigma)],K,\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}}.

By Zariski’s main theorem, Lemma
1.42
and Corollary
1.44
, we have
\Delta^{\circ}_{\Phi,K}\backslash\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi}^{a},\sigma,\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}\cong\mathcal{Z}_{[(\Phi,\sigma)],K,\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}
and
\coprod_{[(\Phi^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime})]\in[ZP^{a}(\Phi^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime})]}\mathcal{Z}_{[(\Phi^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime})],K,\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}\cong\mathcal{Z}_{[ZP^{a}(\Phi,\sigma)],K,\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}
.
Taking the completion with respect to
\mathcal{Z}_{[(\Phi,\sigma)],K,\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}
on both sides of (
4.28
), as we can check that

{(\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}^{a}}(\sigma)_{\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}})}{}^{\wedge{\ }}_{\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}^{a},\sigma,\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}}\cong\coprod_{[(\Phi^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime})]\in[ZP^{a}(\Phi^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime})]}\Delta^{\circ}_{\Phi^{\prime},K}\backslash{(\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi^{\prime}}}(\sigma^{\prime})_{\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}})}{}^{\wedge{\ }}_{\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi^{\prime}},\sigma^{\prime},\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}},

we have

{(\mathcal{S}^{\Sigma}_{K,\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}(G,X_{a}))}{}^{\wedge{\ }}_{\mathcal{Z}_{[(\Phi,\sigma)],K,\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}}\cong\Delta^{\circ}_{\Phi,K}\backslash{(\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi}^{a}}(\sigma)_{\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}})}{}^{\wedge{\ }}_{\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi},\sigma,\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}}.

Since
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{2}}^{\Sigma_{2}^{\prime}}(G_{2},X_{2})\longrightarrow\mathrm{Sh}_{K}^{\Sigma}(G,X_{a})
is an open and closed embedding by Corollary
1.37
, we see that

\Delta^{\circ}_{{\Phi_{2}},K_{2}}\backslash{(\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{2}}}(\sigma_{2})_{\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}})}{}^{\wedge{\ }}_{\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{2}},\sigma_{2},\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}}\cong{(\mathcal{S}^{\Sigma_{2}^{\prime}}_{K_{2},\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}})}{}^{\wedge{\ }}_{\mathcal{Z}_{[(\Phi_{2},\sigma_{2})],K_{2},\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}}.

Applying
(.3)
, the action of
\mathrm{Gal}(E^{\prime}_{K_{Z}}/E^{\prime})
on
\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{2}},\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}
naturally induces actions of
\mathrm{Gal}(E^{\prime}_{K_{Z}}/E^{\prime})
on
\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{2}},\sigma_{2},\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}
and
\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{2}}}(\sigma_{2})_{\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}
.

The actions of
\Delta^{\circ}_{\Phi_{2},K_{2}}
and
\mathrm{Gal}(E^{\prime}_{K_{Z}}/E^{\prime})
commute with each other since it is so over the generic fiber. Finally, by
[
50
, Lem. A.3.4]
, the action of
\mathrm{Gal}(E^{\prime}_{K_{Z}}/E^{\prime})
on
\mathcal{S}_{K_{2},\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}
extends to
\mathcal{S}^{\Sigma_{2}^{\prime}}_{K_{2},\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}
.

Define
\mathcal{S}^{\Sigma_{2}^{\prime}}_{K_{2}}
as the quotient of
\mathcal{S}^{\Sigma_{2}^{\prime}}_{K_{2},\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}
by
\mathrm{Gal}(E^{\prime}_{K_{Z}}/E^{\prime})
(note that the quotient exists and is proper (resp. projective) over
\mathcal{O}^{\prime}_{(v)}
by
[
12
, Thm. 3.1.13]
(resp.
[
38
, Ch. 4, Prop. 1.5]
) again). Then
\mathcal{S}^{\Sigma^{\prime}_{2}}_{K_{2}}
satisfies
(.1)
.

Finally, taking the quotient by
\mathrm{Gal}(E^{\prime}_{K_{Z}}/E^{\prime})
of the isomorphism above, let us show
(.4)
holds and show
(.2)
along the way. Taking Galois quotient induces a quasi-finite morphism
f_{\Upsilon_{2}}:\mathcal{Z}_{[(\Phi_{2},\sigma_{2})],K_{2}}\to\mathcal{S}_{K_{2}}^{\Sigma_{2}^{\prime}}
which is finite onto its image (which is inductively defined as remarked in Lemma
4.21
). Denote by
[\mathcal{Z}_{\Upsilon_{2}}]
the image of
f_{\Upsilon_{2}}
. There is a structural morphism induced by canonical projection to the closed stratum of the torus embedding:

{(\mathcal{S}^{\Sigma_{2}}_{K_{2},\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}})}{}^{\wedge{\ }}_{\mathcal{Z}_{\Upsilon_{2},K_{2},\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}}\cong{(\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{2}},\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}^{*}(\sigma_{2}))}{}^{\wedge{\ }}_{\mathcal{S}^{*}_{K_{\Phi_{2}},\sigma_{2},\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}}\twoheadrightarrow\mathcal{Z}_{\Upsilon_{2},K_{2},\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}},

and the pre-composition of it with
\mathcal{Z}_{\Upsilon_{2},K_{2},\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}\to{(\mathcal{S}^{\Sigma_{2}^{\prime}}_{K_{2},\mathcal{O}_{F_{Z}}})}{}^{\wedge{\ }}_{\mathcal{Z}_{\Upsilon_{2},K_{2},\mathcal{O}_{F_{Z}}}}
is identity.
Then taking Galois quotient induces an isomorphism
f_{\Upsilon_{2}}
. In conclusion, we obtained a stratification of normal and flat subschemes for
\mathcal{S}_{K_{2}}^{\Sigma_{2}^{\prime}}
and also the assertion
(.4)
over
\mathcal{O}^{\prime}_{(v)}
.
∎

Lemma 4.50
.

The statement
(.5)
is true.

Proof.

It follows from Proposition
4.30
(3) and Construction
4.33
that the towers
\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}^{a}_{\Phi,p}}
,
\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{\widetilde{K}^{a}_{\Phi,p}}
and
\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}^{a}_{\Phi,h,p}}
over
\mathcal{O}^{\prime}_{(v)}
have the extension property. By Lemma
1.22
, we see that
\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{2},p}}
(resp.
\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{K_{\Phi_{2},p}}
and resp.
\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{2},h,p}}
) is open and closed in
\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}^{a}_{\Phi,p}}
(resp.
\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{\widetilde{K}^{a}_{\Phi,p}}
and resp.
\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}^{a}_{\Phi,h,p}}
) when
\widetilde{K}^{a,p}_{\Phi}
is sufficiently small. Once we have the statement over
\mathcal{O}^{\prime}_{(v)}
, the descent data from
\mathcal{O}^{\prime}_{(v)}
to
\mathcal{O}_{2}
will extend to the integral models. Thus, we have the statement over
\mathcal{O}_{2}
.
∎

In Case (HS) and its deeper levels, by the extension property
(.5)
and
[
50
, Lem. A.3.4]
and since we have shown all statements over
\mathcal{O}^{\prime}_{(v)}
, we then all results descend to
\mathcal{O}_{2}=\mathcal{O}_{E_{2},(v_{2})}
.
In Case (
\text{STB}_{n}
) and its deeper levels, by construction at the beginning of §
4.2
, for any place
v_{2}|p
of
E_{2}
, we can find a place
v^{\prime}
of
E^{\prime}
over
v_{2}
and find a place
v
of
E_{0}
such that
v^{\prime}|v|p
, and we can establish the results over
\mathcal{O}_{2}=\mathcal{O}_{E_{2},v_{2}}
.

Now we have finished the proof of Theorem
4.39
.
\square

4.3.5.

Let us collect some consequences of Theorem
4.39
.

Corollary 4.51
.

With Theorem
4.39
and all constructions in this section, the following statements are true:

(1)

(See
[
50
, Cor. 4.1.7]
.) When
G_{2}
is
\mathbb{Q}
-anisotropic (i.e.
G_{2}
does not have proper parabolic subgroups over
\mathbb{Q}
, or equivalently,
G_{2}
