does not contain noncentral split torus),
\mathcal{S}_{K_{2}}
is projective over
\mathcal{O}_{2}
. The converse is also true.

(2)

(See
[
45
, Prop. 14.1 and Prop. 14.2]
.) Suppose that
\Sigma_{2}^{\prime}
is smooth. Let
\mathcal{P}
be one of the following properties: (geometrically) reduced, (geometrically) normal, Cohen-Macaulay, (geometrically)
(R_{i})
each one for
i\geq 0
,
(S_{i})
each one for
i\geq 0
, (geometrically) regular. Then a fiber of
\mathcal{S}^{\Sigma_{2}^{\prime}}_{K_{2}}\to\spec\mathcal{O}_{2}
satisfies the property
\mathcal{P}
if and only if the corresponding fiber of
\mathcal{S}_{K_{2}}\to\spec\mathcal{O}_{2}
has the property
\mathcal{P}
. Moreover,
\mathcal{S}_{K_{2}}^{\Sigma_{2}^{\prime}}
is regular (resp. smooth over
\spec\mathcal{O}_{2}
) if and only if
\mathcal{S}_{K_{2}}
is regular (resp. smooth over
\spec\mathcal{O}_{2}
).

(3)

(See
[
45
, Cor. 10.18 and Cor. 14.4]
.) The scheme
\mathcal{S}_{K_{2}}
is fiberwise dense in
\mathcal{S}_{K_{2}}^{\Sigma_{2}^{\prime}}
. If
\mathcal{S}_{K_{2}}\to\spec\mathcal{O}_{2}
has geometrically reduced fibers, so does
\mathcal{S}_{K_{2}}^{\Sigma_{2}^{\prime}}\to\spec\mathcal{O}_{2}
; in this case, the geometric fibers of the morphisms
\mathcal{S}_{K_{2}}\to\spec\mathcal{O}_{2}
and
\mathcal{S}_{K_{2}}^{\Sigma_{2}^{\prime}}\to\spec\mathcal{O}_{2}
have the same number of connected components.

Proof.

The proof is almost identical to that of the references cited. If
G_{2}
is
\mathbb{Q}
-anisotropic, there are no proper
\mathbb{Q}
-parabolic subgroups, and therefore there are no extra strata in toroidal compactifications by
(.2)
. Conversely, if
\mathcal{S}_{K_{2}}
is proper, it is identical to any of its toroidal compactifications by Zariski’s main theorem. Hence, it does not have extra strata in its compactifications and
G_{2}^{\mathrm{ad}}
does not contain admissible
\mathbb{Q}
-parabolic subgroups. Since a proper admissible
\mathbb{Q}
-parabolic subgroup is a product of maximal proper
\mathbb{Q}
-parabolic subgroups in some
\mathbb{Q}
-simple factors of
G^{\mathrm{ad}}_{2}
with the whole groups of other
\mathbb{Q}
-simple factors of
G^{\mathrm{ad}}_{2}
, there are no proper
\mathbb{Q}
-parabolic subgroups contained in
G^{\mathrm{ad}}_{2}
if there are no maximal ones. So part one is true.

Part two follows from exactly the same arguments as in
[
45
, Prop. 14.1 and 14.2]
. Note that, with
(.3)
and
(.4)
in hand, one can replace
\vec{\Xi}_{\Phi_{\mathcal{H}},\delta_{\mathcal{H}}}\to\vec{C}_{\Phi_{\mathcal{H}},\delta_{\mathcal{H}}}
and
\vec{\Xi}_{\Phi_{\mathcal{H}},\delta_{\mathcal{H}}}(\sigma)\to\vec{C}_{\Phi_{\mathcal{H}},\delta_{\mathcal{H}}}
in the proof of
loc. cit.
with
\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{2}}}^{*}\to\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{K_{\Phi_{2}}}^{*}
and
\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{2}}}^{*}(\sigma_{2})\to\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{K_{\Phi_{2}}}^{*}
.

It follows from
(.3)
and
(.4)
that
\mathcal{S}_{K_{2}}
is fiberwise dense in
\mathcal{S}_{K_{2}}^{\Sigma_{2}^{\prime}}
. Then the rest of the third part follows from

[
71
,
Lem. 0E0N
]
and
(.1)
.

Note that for both the second and the third parts, we needed to use the fact that the isomorphisms in
(.4)
preserve the open dense strata corresponding to
\mathcal{S}_{K_{2}}\subset\mathcal{S}_{K_{2}}^{\Sigma_{2}^{\prime}}
and
\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{2}}}^{*}\subset\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{2}}}^{*}(\sigma_{2})
, and this fact follows from our quotient construction and from the corresponding fact in the Hodge-type case.
∎

Remark 4.52
.

For the definition of being
\mathbb{Q}
-anisotropic, we follow
[
70
, Prop. 16.2.2]
. This definition is equivalent to “being
\mathbb{Q}
-anisotropic modulo center” in many other references where a reductive group is called
\mathbb{Q}
-anisotropic if it does not contain any split
\mathbb{Q}
-torus.

4.3.6.

The following statements follow from a combination of the main results and the Artin approximation technique (and its generalizations).

The next proposition is a formal consequence of having a
strata-preserving
toroidal embedding.

Proposition 4.53
.

(See
[
42
, Prop. 2.2(9)]
and
[
40
, Prop. 2.1.2(9)]
) Let
x
be a point of
\mathcal{Z}_{[(\Phi_{2},\sigma_{2})],K_{2}}\cong\mathcal{S}^{*}_{K_{\Phi_{2}},\sigma_{2}}
. There is an étale neighborhood
\mathsf{e}_{1}:\overline{U}\to\mathcal{S}_{K_{2}}^{\Sigma_{2}^{\prime}}
of
x
, and an étale morphism
\mathsf{e}_{2}:\overline{U}\to\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{2}}}^{*}(\sigma_{2})
, such that the stratifications on
\overline{U}
defined by pulling back those on
\mathcal{S}_{K_{2}}^{\Sigma_{2}^{\prime}}
and
\mathcal{S}^{*}_{K_{\Phi_{2}}}(\sigma_{2})
via
\mathsf{e}_{1}
and
\mathsf{e}_{2}
coincide. In particular, we have
\mathsf{e}_{1}^{-1}(\mathcal{S}_{K_{2}})=\mathsf{e}_{2}^{-1}(\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{2}}}^{*})
.

Proof.

Let
A
be the local ring
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{S}^{*}_{K_{\Phi_{2}}}(\sigma_{2}),x}
at
x
with Henselization denoted by
A^{h}
. Denote by
\widehat{A}
the complete local ring of
A^{h}
at
x
. Let
\widetilde{A}
be the finitely generated
A^{h}
-algebra generated by the local ring
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{S}^{\Sigma_{2}^{\prime}}_{K_{2}},x}
via the last isomorphism in
(.4)
.
Then there is a sequence of inclusions
A\xhookrightarrow{i}A^{h}\xhookrightarrow{\alpha}\widetilde{A}\xhookrightarrow{\beta}\widehat{A}
. By Artin approximation
[
4
, Thm. 1.10]
(cf.
[
44
, Prop. 6.3.2.2]
), for any integer
k>0
, there is a section
\alpha_{k}:\widetilde{A}\to A^{h}
, such that
\alpha_{k}\circ\alpha=\mathrm{Id}
and
\beta\circ\alpha\circ\alpha_{k}-\beta
is trivial modulo
\mathfrak{m}^{k}_{x}
, where
\mathfrak{m}_{x}
is the maximal ideal in
\widehat{A}
corresponding to
x
. For
k\geq 2
,
\alpha_{k}
defines a scheme
\overline{U}
with étale morphisms
\mathsf{e}_{1}
and
\mathsf{e}_{2}
to
\mathcal{S}_{K_{2}}^{\Sigma_{2}^{\prime}}
and
\mathcal{S}^{*}_{K_{\Phi_{2}}}(\sigma_{2})
by
[
4
, Cor. 2.6]
.

Now we modify the construction above to get strata-preserving
(\overline{U},\mathsf{e}_{1},\mathsf{e}_{2})
. Note that using induction it is enough to show it for closures of strata. Let
\overline{\mathcal{M}}
be the closure of a stratum
\mathcal{M}
in
\mathcal{S}^{\Sigma_{2}^{\prime}}_{K_{2}}
which under the last isomorphism in
(.4)
, corresponds to
\overline{\mathcal{N}}
, the closure in
\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{2}}}(\sigma_{2})
of a stratum
\mathcal{N}
. Suppose that the pullback of
\overline{\mathcal{N}}
to
\spec A
determines an ideal
I\subset A
and the pullback of
\overline{\mathcal{M}}
to
\spec\widetilde{A}
determines an ideal
J\subset\widetilde{A}
. We have
\beta\circ\alpha\circ i(I)\widehat{A}=\beta(J)\widehat{A}
.
Since
\widehat{A}
is Noetherian, choose a finite set of elements
\{x_{t}^{\mathcal{M}}\}_{t\in\Lambda_{\mathcal{M}}}\subset\widehat{A}
such that
\beta(J)\subset\sum_{t\in\Lambda_{\mathcal{M}}}I\widetilde{A}x_{t}^{\mathcal{M}}
, and a finite set of
\{y_{s}^{\mathcal{M}}\}_{s\in\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{M}}}\subset\widehat{A}
such that
\beta\circ\alpha\circ i(I)\subset\sum_{s\in\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{M}}}J\widetilde{A}y_{s}^{\mathcal{M}}
. Let
\widetilde{B}
be the
A^{h}
-algebra generated by
\widetilde{A}
and all
\{x_{t}^{\mathcal{M}}\}_{t\in\Lambda_{\mathcal{M}}}
and
\{y_{s}^{\mathcal{M}}\}_{s\in\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{M}}}
running over all strata
\mathcal{M}
of
\mathcal{S}^{\Sigma_{2}^{\prime}}_{K_{2}}
. The algebra
\widetilde{B}
is still finitely generated over
A^{h}
, and there is a sequence of inclusions
A\xhookrightarrow{i}A^{h}\xhookrightarrow{\widetilde{\alpha}}\widetilde{B}\xhookrightarrow{\widetilde{\beta}}\widehat{A}
. By Artin approximation as above, for any integer
k\geq 2
, there is a section
\widetilde{\alpha}_{k}:\widetilde{B}\to A^{h}
, such that
\widetilde{\alpha}_{k}\circ\widetilde{\alpha}=\mathrm{Id}
,
\widetilde{\beta}\circ\widetilde{\alpha}\circ\widetilde{\alpha}_{k}-\widetilde{\beta}
is trivial modulo
\mathfrak{m}^{k}_{x}
, and
\alpha_{k}:=\widetilde{\alpha}_{k}|_{\widetilde{A}}
defines a scheme
\overline{U}
with étale morphisms
\mathsf{e}_{1}
and
\mathsf{e}_{2}
to
\mathcal{S}_{K_{2}}^{\Sigma_{2}^{\prime}}
and
\mathcal{S}^{*}_{K_{\Phi_{2}}}(\sigma_{2})
.
Since for any
j\in J
,
(\beta\circ\alpha\circ\alpha_{k}-\beta)(j)\subset(\widetilde{\beta}\circ\widetilde{\alpha}\circ\widetilde{\alpha}_{k}-\widetilde{\beta})(\sum I\widetilde{A}x_{t}^{\mathcal{M}})=\sum I(\widetilde{\beta}\circ\widetilde{\alpha}\circ\widetilde{\alpha}_{k}-\widetilde{\beta})(\widetilde{A}x_{t}^{\mathcal{M}})\subset I\widehat{A}
and
\alpha\circ\alpha_{k}\circ\alpha\circ i(I)=\alpha\circ i(I)
, we have
I\widehat{A}=\beta(J)\widehat{A}=\beta\circ\alpha\circ\alpha_{k}(J)\widehat{A}
, as desired.
∎

From Proposition
4.53
above, we see that

Corollary 4.54
.

The complement of
\mathcal{S}_{K_{2}}
in
\mathcal{S}_{K_{2}}^{\Sigma_{2}^{\prime}}
is a relative effective Cartier divisor.

Corollary 4.55
.

With the constructions and conventions above,
after taking the normalized base change to
\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}
,
\mathcal{S}_{K_{2},\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}^{\Sigma_{2}^{\prime}}
is an open and closed subspace of
\mathcal{S}_{K,\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}^{\Sigma}(G,X_{b})
, and
\mathcal{S}_{K,\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}^{\Sigma}(G,X_{b})
, equipped with a log structure defined by the complement of
\mathcal{S}_{K,\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}(G,X_{b})
in
\mathcal{S}_{K,\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}^{\Sigma}(G,X_{b})
, admits a finite Kummer étale cover of disjoint union of Hodge-type toroidal compactifications
\mathcal{S}^{\mathrm{TOR}}_{K_{0},\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}:=\coprod_{\alpha\in I_{G/G_{0}}}\mathcal{S}^{\Sigma_{0}^{\alpha}}_{K_{0}^{\alpha},\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}
. The induced map between interiors
\coprod_{\alpha\in I_{G/G_{0}}}\mathcal{S}_{K_{0}^{\alpha},\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}\to\mathcal{S}_{K,\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}(G,X_{b})
is finite étale.

Proof.

It remains to show that the morphism from
\mathcal{S}_{K_{0},\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}^{\mathrm{TOR}}
to
\mathcal{S}^{\Sigma}_{K,\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}
is Kummer étale.
First, we apply the nested approximation theorem due to Teissier and Spivakovsky, see
[
73
, Cor. 2.10]
and
[
69
, Thm. 11.5]
, to find compatible approximations of a morphism of completions. Let us explain the exact meanings of the symbols in
loc. cit.
specialized to our situation.
The
m
in
loc. cit.
is
2
in our situation. Pick any point
x\in\mathcal{Z}:=\mathcal{Z}_{[ZP^{b}(\Phi,\sigma)],K,\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}
and let
A_{1}
in
loc. cit.
be the Henselization of the local ring of
\mathcal{S}^{\Sigma}_{K,\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}
at
x
. The pullback of
\mathcal{S}_{K,\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}
to
\spec A_{1}
determines an ideal
I\subset A_{1}
. Let
B_{1}
be the completion of
A_{1}
with respect to the maximal ideal
\mathfrak{m}_{x}\subset A_{1}
corresponding to
x
.
The point
x
corresponds to a point
x^{\prime}
in
\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}^{b},\sigma,\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}
, and let
\mathcal{O}
be the local ring of
\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}^{b}_{\Phi},\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}(\sigma)
at
x^{\prime}
.
The pullback of
\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}^{b}_{\Phi},\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}
to
\spec\mathcal{O}
determines an ideal
J\subset\mathcal{O}
. We have
JB_{1}=IB_{1}
. Choose a finite set of elements
\{x_{t}\}_{t\in\Lambda}
in
B_{1}
such that
J\subset\sum_{t\in\Lambda}I\mathcal{O}\cdot x_{t}
and a finite set of elements
\{y_{s}\}_{s\in\mathcal{I}}
in
B_{1}
such that
I\subset\sum_{s\in\mathcal{I}}J\mathcal{O}\cdot y_{s}
.
Let
C_{1}
be the finitely generated
A_{1}
-algebra generated by
\{x_{t}\}
and
\{y_{t}\}
and
\mathcal{O}
via (
4.21
). Let
A_{2}
(resp.
B_{2}
) be the algebra obtained by tensoring
\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{S}^{\Sigma}_{K,\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}}}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{S}^{\mathrm{TOR}}_{K_{0},\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}}
to
A_{1}
(resp.
B_{1}
).
Let
C_{2}
be the finitely generated
A_{2}
-algebra generated by
\{x_{t}\}
,
\{y_{s}\}
and a semi-local ring of the disjoint union of
\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{0}^{\alpha}},\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}(\sigma_{0}^{\alpha})
, corresponding to all cusp labels with cones mapping to
[ZP^{b}(\Phi,\sigma)]
(cf. (
4.15
)), at the inverse image of
x
in
\mathcal{S}^{\mathrm{TOR}}_{K_{0},\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}
. Since completion and Henselization commute with finite ring maps, we can apply
loc. cit.
to find compatible approximations.
More precisely (cf.
[
47
, Cor. 3.8]
), there is an étale neighborhood
\overline{U}
of
x
and an étale morphism
\overline{U}\to\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}^{b}_{\Phi},\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}(\sigma)
such that the preimage
U
in
\overline{U}
of
\mathcal{S}_{K,\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}
coincides with the preimage of
\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}^{b}_{\Phi},\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}
. Moreover, the étale morphisms
\overline{U}\to\mathcal{S}^{\Sigma}_{K,\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}
and
\overline{U}\to\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}^{b}_{\Phi},\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}(\sigma)
induce étale morphisms
\overline{U}_{0}\to\mathcal{S}^{\mathrm{TOR}}_{K_{0},\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}
and
\overline{U}_{0}\to\coprod\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{0}^{\alpha}},\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}(\sigma_{0}^{\alpha})
by pulling back via
\mathcal{S}^{\mathrm{TOR}}_{K_{0},\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}\to\mathcal{S}^{\Sigma}_{K,\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}
and
\coprod\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{0}^{\alpha}},\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}(\sigma_{0}^{\alpha})\to\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}^{b}_{\Phi},\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}(\sigma)
, respectively. The two étale morphisms obtained by pullback as before have the property that the preimages of
\coprod_{I_{G/G_{0}}}\mathcal{S}_{K_{0}^{\alpha},\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}
and
\coprod\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{0}^{\alpha}},\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}
coincide with the preimage
U_{0}
of
U\subset\overline{U}
via
\overline{U}_{0}\to\overline{U}
.

Now, thanks to Proposition
4.29
, it suffices to show that each
\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{0}^{\alpha}}}(\sigma_{0}^{\alpha})\to\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}^{b}_{\Phi}}(\sigma)
is Kummer étale, where the log structures on the source and the target are defined by the torus embeddings. By Lemma
4.25
and Proposition
4.30
, the morphism
\mathbf{E}_{K_{\Phi_{0}^{\alpha}}}\to\mathbf{E}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}}
between tori induced by
\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{0}^{\alpha}}}\to\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}^{b}_{\Phi}}
is a prime-to-
p
isogeny. Since
\sigma_{0}^{\alpha}
is induced by
\sigma
, the map
\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{0}^{\alpha}}}(\sigma_{0}^{\alpha})\to\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}^{b}_{\Phi}}(\sigma)
is log étale and Kummer, as desired.
∎

4.3.7.

The main result in §
4.3.7
concerns when the proper morphism
\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{K_{\Phi_{2}}}^{*}\to\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{2}},h}^{*}
is an abelian scheme torsor.

Consider (DL) of (
\mathrm{STB}_{n}
). Suppose
K_{2,p}\subset K_{2,p}^{\prime}
, where
K_{2,p}^{\prime}
is an intersection of
n
Bruhat-Tits stabilizer subgroups
K_{2,p}^{i}\subset G_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
for
1\leq i\leq n
.
Denote by
K_{2,p}^{i,\circ}
the parahoric subgroups in
G_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
associated with
K_{2,p}^{i}
.
Assume
in §
4.3.7
that

(4.29)

K_{2,p}^{\circ}:=\cap_{i=1}^{n}K_{2,p}^{i,\circ}\subset K_{2,p}\subset K_{2,p}^{\prime}.

Lemma 4.56

(See
[
51
, Lem. 3.24 and Cor. 3.56]
)
.

Assume that
(G_{2},X_{2})=(G_{0},X_{0})
. Let
K^{p}_{0}
be any neat open compact subgroup of
G_{0}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
,
g
be any element in
G_{0}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
and
\Phi_{0}\in\mathcal{CLR}(G_{0},X_{0})
. Under the assumption above, we have

\overline{gK_{0,p}^{\circ}K_{0}^{p}g^{-1}\cap W_{\Phi_{0}}(\mathbb{A}_{f})}=\overline{gK_{0,p}^{\prime}K_{0}^{p}g^{-1}\cap W_{\Phi_{0}}(\mathbb{A}_{f})},

where the “
\overline{\ }
” denotes the projection to
V_{\Phi_{0}}(\mathbb{A}_{f}):=W_{\Phi_{0}}(\mathbb{A}_{f})/U_{\Phi_{0}}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
.

Proof.

In
[
51
, Lem. 3.24]
, it is shown when
n=1
that
gK_{0,p}^{\circ}K_{0}^{p}g^{-1}\cap W_{\Phi_{0}}(\mathbb{A}_{f})=gK_{0,p}^{\prime}K_{0}^{p}g^{-1}\cap W_{\Phi_{0}}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
. The rest of the lemma follows from taking the intersection and projection.
∎

We need the following technical lemma:

Lemma 4.57
.

Fixing
K_{2,p}
,
the construction of
\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{2}}}\to\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{K_{\Phi_{2}}}\to\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{2}},h}
in Theorem
4.39
does not depend on the choice of
K_{p}\subset G(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
made at the beginning of §
4.3.1
.

Under Assumption (
4.29
),

•

we can choose an open compact subgroup
K_{p}
such that, for any
\alpha
,
\pi^{b,-1}({}^{\alpha}{K_{p}})
is an intersection of quasi-parahoric subgroups corresponding to the same points as
{}^{\alpha}{K^{i}_{2,p}}
’s in the Bruhat-Tits building of
G^{\mathrm{ad}}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
;

•

with this
K_{p}
, we can choose
\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}
to be unramified over
\mathcal{O}^{\prime}_{(v)}
.

Proof.

The first sentence follows from the construction that the integral models such as
\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{2}}}
are normalizations from the corresponding integral models in Case (
\mathrm{STB}_{n}
).

We now construct a
K_{p}
to satisfy the first item. In Case (
\mathrm{STB}_{n}
),
K_{p}
is chosen to be an intersection of Bruhat-Tits stabilizers that correspond to the same points as
K^{i}_{2,p}
’s in the Bruhat-Tits building of
G^{\mathrm{ad}}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
. Under only Assumption (
4.29
), it is reduced to find such a
K_{p}
when
K_{2,p}
is quasi-parahoric as one can take an intersection after this. When
K_{2,p}
is quasi-parahoric, denote by
K_{0,p}
the parahoric subgroup of
G_{0}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
such that
K_{2,p}
and
K_{0,p}
correspond to the same point in the Bruhat-Tits building of
G^{\mathrm{ad}}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
. Note that
K_{p}^{\bullet}:=\pi^{b}(K_{0,p})\cdot\pi^{a}(K_{2,p})
is a compact group in
G(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
and
K_{p}^{\bullet}\cap G_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})=K_{2,p}
. We then find an open compact subgroup
K_{p}
in the Bruhat-Tits stabilizer subgroup of
G(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
corresponding to
K_{2,p}
containing
K_{p}^{\bullet}
such that
K_{p}\cap G_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})=K_{2,p}
. The preimage
\pi^{b,-1}(K_{p})
contains
K_{0,p}
and is contained in the Bruhat-Tits stabilizer corresponding to
K_{0,p}
. Taking conjugates of
g_{\alpha}
, we get the desired result.

We now explain the second item. Again, we assume that
K_{2,p}
is quasi-parahoric first. Pick any point
x\in X_{a}\cong X_{b}\cong X^{\mathrm{ad}}
and denote by
\mu_{a}
(resp.
\mu_{b}
) its associated Hodge cocharacter regarded as a point on
X_{a}
(resp.
X_{b}
). By the proof of
[
33
, Lem. 4.3.2]
, the image of
\mathcal{O}_{E^{\prime}_{v^{\prime}}}^{\times}
under (
4.7
) induced by
\mu_{a}
(resp.
\mu_{b}
) is contained in
\pi^{a}(K_{2,p})
(resp.
\pi^{b}(K_{0,p})
) up to a conjugation. As
\mu_{a}\cdot\mu_{b}^{-1}
is central, the images of
\mathcal{O}_{E^{\prime}_{v^{\prime}}}^{\times}
under (
4.7
) induced by
\mu_{a}
and
\mu_{b}
are contained in
\pi^{a}(K_{2,p})
and
\pi^{b}(K_{0,p})
respectively after the same conjugation, and the image of it under (
4.7
) induced by
\mu_{a}\cdot\mu_{b}^{-1}
is contained in
K_{p}^{\bullet}\cap Z_{G}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\subset K_{p}\cap Z_{G}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
. By taking intersection, we have shown the second item.
∎

Remark 4.58
.

When
p>2
, we expect that one can also show the statement above using the machinery developed in Daniels-Youcis
[
14
]
and Kisin-Pappas-Zhou
[
32
]
. See, e.g.,
[
14
, Prop. 2.15 and Prop. 5.4]
.

Proposition 4.59
.

Under Assumption (
4.29
),
\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{K_{\Phi_{2}}}^{*}\to\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{2}},h}^{*}
is an abelian scheme torsor.

Proof.

Under the assumption, we can choose
E^{\prime}_{K_{Z}}
unramified over
E^{\prime}
at
p
by Lemma
4.57
. Hence, by Part
2
of Proposition
4.30
it is enough to show the statement for
\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{\widetilde{K}^{b}_{\Phi}}\to\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}^{b}_{\Phi},h}
.
By
[
50
, 2.1.10, Thm. 4.1.5(1)]
, Lemma
4.56
and Zariski’s main theorem, all
\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{K_{\Phi_{\beta^{\prime}}}}\to\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{\beta^{\prime}}},h}
used to construct
\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{\widetilde{K}^{b}_{\Phi}}\to\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}^{b}_{\Phi},h}
are abelian scheme torsors.
We then employ the proof of Part
1
of Proposition
4.30
to obtain the desired result.
∎

5.
Minimal compactifications of integral models of abelian type

In this section, we construct the minimal compactification
\mathcal{S}^{\mathrm{min}}_{K_{2}}
of
\mathcal{S}_{K_{2}}
along with the stratification on it. We still assume that
K_{2}=K_{2,p}K^{p}_{2}
, where
K^{p}_{2}
is a neat open compact subgroup of
G_{2}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
and
K_{2,p}
is an open compact subgroup of
G_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
and regard
(G_{2},X_{2},K_{2,p})
as one of the Cases (HS), (
\text{STB}_{n}
) and (DL). To avoid overloaded notation, we assume
\Sigma_{2}=\Sigma_{2}^{\prime}
.

5.1.
Minimal compactifications of integral models of Hodge type

Fix a Hodge-type Shimura datum
(G_{0},X_{0})
with an embedding
\iota:(G_{0},X_{0})\hookrightarrow(G^{\ddagger},X^{\ddagger})
. Recall that
E_{0}=E(G_{0},X_{0})
,
v
is a place of
E_{0}
over
p
, and all integral models related to
(G_{0},X_{0})
are defined over
\mathcal{O}_{E_{0},(v)}
. Let us first briefly recall the construction of the minimal compactification
\mathcal{S}_{K_{0}}^{\mathrm{min}}
for
\mathcal{S}_{K_{0}}
(see
[
50
, Sec. 5]
). We then deduce a functoriality result analogous to Corollary
4.11
and Lemma
4.14
for minimal compactifications.

5.1.1.

As explained in §
2.1
, the integral model
\mathcal{S}_{K^{{\ddagger}}_{p}K^{{\ddagger},p}}
of Siegel type is associated with a moduli problem
\mathbf{M}^{\mathrm{iso}}_{(V_{\mathbb{Z}},\psi_{\mathbb{Z}}),K^{{\ddagger}}_{p}K^{{\ddagger},p}}
. Denote by
(\mathcal{A}^{{\ddagger}},\lambda,[\varepsilon]_{K^{{\ddagger},p}})
the universal polarized abelian scheme with level structure on
\mathcal{S}_{K^{{\ddagger}}_{p}K^{{\ddagger},p}}
. Denote the
Hodge invertible sheaf
associated with
\mathcal{A}^{{\ddagger}}
by
\omega_{\mathcal{A}^{{\ddagger}}/\mathcal{S}_{K^{{\ddagger}}}}:=(\wedge^{\mathrm{top}}\underline{\mathrm{Lie}}_{\mathcal{A}^{{\ddagger}}/\mathcal{S}_{K^{{\ddagger}}}})^{\otimes-1}
; the pullback
\omega_{K_{0}}:=\iota^{*}\omega_{\mathcal{A}^{\ddagger}/\mathcal{S}_{K^{\ddagger}}}
of it to
\mathcal{S}_{K_{0}}
is called a Hodge invertible sheaf on
\mathcal{S}_{K_{0}}
. Then
\omega_{\mathcal{A}^{\ddagger}/\mathcal{S}_{K^{\ddagger}}}
extends to an invertible sheaf
\omega_{K^{\ddagger}}^{\mathrm{tor}}:=(\wedge^{\mathrm{top}}\underline{\mathrm{Lie}}_{\mathcal{G}^{\ddagger}/\mathcal{S}_{K^{\ddagger}}})^{\otimes-1}
, where
\mathcal{G}^{\ddagger}
is the degenerating semi-abelian scheme over
\mathcal{S}_{K^{\ddagger}}^{\Sigma^{\ddagger}}
extending
\mathcal{A}^{\ddagger}
; the pullback of it to
\mathcal{S}_{K_{0}}^{\Sigma_{0}}
via
\iota:\mathcal{S}_{K_{0}}^{\Sigma_{0}}\to\mathcal{S}_{K^{\ddagger}}^{\Sigma^{\ddagger}}
is an invertible sheaf extending
\omega_{K_{0}}
, denoted by
\omega_{K_{0}}^{\mathrm{tor}}
.

Construction 5.1

(
[
50
, Sec. 5.2]
)
.

For a sufficiently large integer
k_{0}
, the tensor power
\omega_{K_{0}}^{\mathrm{tor},\otimes k_{0}}
is generated by its global sections. Then there is a morphism
\int_{K_{0}}:\mathcal{S}_{K_{0}}^{\Sigma_{0}}\to\mathbb{P}^{M}
defined by the global sections of
\omega_{K_{0}}^{\mathrm{tor},\otimes k_{0}}
.
The minimal compactification of
\mathcal{S}_{K_{0}}
is defined to be

\mathcal{S}_{K_{0}}^{\mathrm{min}}:=\mathrm{Proj}(\bigoplus\limits_{k=0}^{\infty}\Gamma(\mathcal{S}_{K_{0}}^{\Sigma_{0}},\omega^{\mathrm{tor},\otimes k}_{K_{0}}))\cong\mathrm{Proj}(\bigoplus\limits_{k=0}^{\infty}\Gamma(\mathcal{S}_{K_{0}}^{\Sigma_{0}},\omega^{\mathrm{tor},\otimes k_{0}k}_{K_{0}}))\cong\underline{\mathrm{Spec}}_{\mathbb{P}^{M}}\int_{K_{0},*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{S}_{K_{0}}^{\Sigma_{0}}}.

Then there is a proper morphism

\oint_{K_{0}}^{\Sigma_{0}}:\mathcal{S}_{K_{0}}^{\Sigma_{0}}\longrightarrow\mathcal{S}_{K_{0}}^{\mathrm{min}}

and a finite morphism

\overline{\oint}_{K_{0}}:\mathcal{S}_{K_{0}}^{\mathrm{min}}\longrightarrow\mathbb{P}^{M}.

By general properties of Stein factorizations (see, e.g.,
[
71
,
Thm. 0A1B
]
), we have that
\oint_{K_{0}}^{\Sigma_{0}}
is proper and surjective, the fibers of
\oint_{K_{0}}^{\Sigma_{0}}
are geometrically connected and
\oint_{K_{0}}^{\Sigma_{0},\sharp}:\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{S}^{\mathrm{min}}_{K_{0}}}\to\oint_{K_{0},*}^{\Sigma_{0}}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{S}_{K_{0}}^{\Sigma_{0}}}
is an isomorphism. Moreover, this construction is independent of the choice of
\Sigma_{0}
.
\square

Theorem 5.2

(
[
50
, Thm. 5.2.11]
; cf.
[
21
, Thm. V.2.5]
and
[
44
, Thm. 7.2.4.1]
)
.

With the conventions and constructions above, there is a natural stratification on
\mathcal{S}_{K_{0}}^{\mathrm{min}}
consisting of locally closed normal subschemes that are flat over
\mathcal{O}_{E_{0},(v)}
, that is,
\mathcal{S}_{K_{0}}^{\mathrm{min}}=\coprod_{[\Phi_{0}]\in\mathrm{Cusp}_{K_{0}}(G_{0},X_{0})}\mathcal{Z}_{[\Phi_{0}],K_{0}}
. The strata are labeled by equivalence classes in
\mathrm{Cusp}_{K_{0}}(G_{0},X_{0})
; each
\mathcal{Z}_{[\Phi_{0}],K_{0}}
is naturally isomorphic to
\Delta_{\Phi_{0},K_{0}}\backslash\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{0}},h}
. A stratum
\mathcal{Z}_{[\Phi_{0}^{\prime}],K_{0}}
lies in the closure of
\mathcal{Z}_{[\Phi_{0}],K_{0}}
if and only if
[\Phi_{0}]^{\prime}\preceq[\Phi_{0}]
.

Moreover, the morphism
\oint_{K_{0}}^{\Sigma_{0}}
is compatible with stratifications in the following sense: The restriction of
\oint_{K_{0}}^{\Sigma_{0}}
to each
\mathcal{Z}_{[(\Phi_{0},\sigma_{0})],K_{0}}
is the natural projection
\mathcal{Z}_{[(\Phi_{0},\sigma_{0})],K_{0}}\cong\Delta^{\circ}_{\Phi_{0},K_{0}}\backslash\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{0}},\sigma_{0}}\to\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{0}},h}\to\Delta_{\Phi_{0},K_{0}}\backslash\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{0}},h}\cong\mathcal{Z}_{[\Phi_{0}],K_{0}}
; the inverse image of each
\mathcal{Z}_{[\Phi_{0}],K_{0}}
under
\oint_{K_{0}}^{\Sigma_{0}}
is
\coprod_{\Upsilon_{0}\in\mathrm{Cusp}_{K_{0}}(G_{0},X_{0})\mapsto[\Phi_{0}]}\mathcal{Z}_{\Upsilon_{0},K_{0}}
.

The Hodge invertible sheaf
\omega_{K_{0}}
extends to an ample invertible sheaf
\omega_{K_{0}}^{\mathrm{min}}
on
\mathcal{S}_{K_{0}}^{\mathrm{min}}
such that
\oint_{K_{0}}^{\Sigma_{0},*}\omega_{K_{0}}^{\mathrm{min}}\cong\omega_{K_{0}}^{\mathrm{tor}}
.

For normalized base change, we have

Proposition 5.3
.

Let
F
be any finite field extension of
E_{0}
. Let
\mathcal{S}^{\mathrm{min}}_{K_{0},\mathcal{O}_{F}}
be the normalization of the base change
\mathcal{S}_{K_{0}}^{\mathrm{min}}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{E,(v)}}\mathcal{O}_{E_{0},(v)}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{E_{0}}}\mathcal{O}_{F}
. Then
\mathcal{S}^{\mathrm{min}}_{K_{0},\mathcal{O}_{F}}
is constructed as

\mathrm{Proj}(\bigoplus\limits_{k=0}^{\infty}\Gamma(\mathcal{S}_{K_{0},\mathcal{O}_{F}}^{\Sigma_{0}},\omega^{\mathrm{tor},\otimes k}_{K_{0},\mathcal{O}_{F}}))

where
\mathcal{S}_{K_{0},\mathcal{O}_{F}}^{\Sigma_{0}}
denotes the normalized base change of
\mathcal{S}_{K_{0}}^{\Sigma_{0}}
to
\mathcal{O}_{E_{0},(v)}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{E_{0}}}\mathcal{O}_{F}
and
\omega^{\mathrm{tor}}_{K_{0},\mathcal{O}_{F}}
denotes the pullback of
\omega^{\mathrm{tor}}_{K_{0}}
to
\mathcal{S}^{\Sigma_{0}}_{K_{0},\mathcal{O}_{F}}
. Moreover, the properties in Theorem
5.2
are also true for
\mathcal{S}^{\mathrm{min}}_{K_{0},\mathcal{O}_{F}}
, with all strata replaced with normalized base change to
\mathcal{O}_{E_{0},(v)}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{E_{0}}}\mathcal{O}_{F}
.

Proof.

Repeat the construction in
[
50
, Sec. 5]
. Note that one can also use the argument in Proposition
2.48
with
[
50
, Cor. 5.2.8]
instead of
[
50
, Thm. 4.1.5 (4)]
as an input.
∎

5.1.2.

Let
(G_{0},X_{0})
be any Shimura datum. The canonical sheaf
\varpi_{0}
of
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{0}}(G_{0},X_{0})
extends to an ample invertible sheaf
\varpi_{0}^{\mathrm{min}}
on
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{0}}^{\mathrm{min}}
and an invertible sheaf
\varpi_{0}^{\mathrm{tor}}
on
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{0}}^{\Sigma_{0}}
, which is locally defined as in
[
64
, 5.26]
. In fact,
\varpi_{0}^{\mathrm{tor}}=\wedge^{\mathrm{top}}\Omega^{1}_{\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{0}}^{\Sigma_{0}}/\spec E_{0}}(\log D)
, the sheaf of meromorphic differential forms with top degree and logarithmic poles along the boundary divisor, and
\varpi_{0}^{\mathrm{tor}}
is isomorphic to the pullback of
\varpi_{0}^{\mathrm{min}}
along the natural projection
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{0}}^{\Sigma_{0}}\to\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{0}}^{\mathrm{min}}
(see
[
60
, Prop. 3.4(b)]
).
Moreover, by a similar construction as in
5.1
, there is a natural isomorphism
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{0}}^{\mathrm{min}}\cong\mathrm{Proj}\bigoplus_{k=0}^{\infty}\Gamma(\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{0}}^{\Sigma_{0}},\varpi_{0}^{\mathrm{tor},\otimes k})
(see
[
64
, 8.2]
and
[
50
, Thm. 5.2.11 (5)]
; see also the proof of
[
43
, Thm. 5.1.1]
). In particular, the natural projection
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{0}}^{\Sigma_{0}}\to\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{0}}^{\mathrm{min}}
is proper, surjective and of connected geometric fibers by general properties of Stein factorization again.

The adjoint Shimura datum
(G^{\mathrm{ad}},X^{\mathrm{ad}})
admits a decomposition
(G^{\mathrm{ad}},X^{\mathrm{ad}})=\prod_{i=1}^{k}(G^{i},X^{i})
, where
G_{i}
are
\mathbb{Q}
-simple factors of
G^{\mathrm{ad}}
. Choose suitable neat open compact subgroups
K^{i}\subset G^{i}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
such that there is a (finite étale) morphism
\pi_{\mathrm{ad}}:\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{0}}(G_{0},X_{0})\to\prod_{i=1}^{k}\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{i}}(G^{i},X^{i})
.
Denote by
\varpi_{i}
the canonical sheaf of
\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{i}}(G^{i},X^{i})
.

Now resume the assumptions in §
5.1.1
that
(G_{0},X_{0})
is of Hodge type.

It is explained in
[
43
, Thm. 5.1.1]
that, for all PEL-type
(G^{\ddagger},X^{\ddagger})
, the construction of
\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{\ddagger}}^{\mathrm{min}}
as above using the extension of the canonical sheaf with log poles on
\mathrm{Sh}^{\Sigma^{\ddagger}}_{K^{\ddagger}}\backslash\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{\ddagger}}
coincides with the generic fiber of
\mathcal{S}_{K^{\ddagger}}^{\mathrm{min}}
constructed as in
[
44
]
using the extension of the Hodge invertible sheaf. Hence, there is a natural finite morphism
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{0}}^{\mathrm{min}}\to\mathcal{S}_{K^{\ddagger}}^{\mathrm{min}}
defined as post-composing the natural morphism
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{0}}^{\mathrm{min}}\to\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{\ddagger}}^{\mathrm{min}}
with the natural embedding
\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{\ddagger}}^{\mathrm{min}}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{S}_{K^{\ddagger}}^{\mathrm{min}}
.

The following property follows directly from
\iota
and Construction
5.1
itself; in other words, it can be shown without knowing the main theorem
[
50
, Thm. 5.2.11]
or its proof:

Proposition 5.4
.

With the conventions above, there are positive integers
N
and
N_{i}
for
1\leq i\leq k
such that
\pi_{\mathrm{ad}}^{*}(\boxtimes_{i=1}^{k}(\varpi_{i}^{\otimes N_{i}}))\cong\omega_{K_{0},\mathbb{Q}}^{\otimes N}
.

Moreover, we can also construct
\mathcal{S}_{K_{0}}^{\mathrm{min}}
as the normalization in

\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{0}}
,
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{0}}^{\Sigma_{0}}
or
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{0}}^{\mathrm{min}}
of the minimal compactification
\mathcal{S}_{K^{\ddagger}}^{\mathrm{min}}
of
\mathcal{S}_{K^{\ddagger}}
constructed in
[
21
, V.2]
and
[
44
, 7.2.3]
, and construct
\oint_{K_{0}}^{\Sigma_{0}}
as the morphism induced by the normalization in
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{0}}
of the morphism
\oint_{K^{\ddagger}}^{\Sigma^{\ddagger}}:\mathcal{S}_{K^{\ddagger}}^{\Sigma^{\ddagger}}\to\mathcal{S}_{K^{\ddagger}}^{\mathrm{min}}
. In particular, the generic fiber of
\mathcal{S}_{K_{0}}^{\mathrm{min}}
is
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{0}}^{\mathrm{min}}
.

Proof.

The first assertion follows from the fifth paragraph of the proof of
[
41
, Prop. 2.4]
.

Let us show the second paragraph. Indeed, the morphism
\oint_{K_{0}}^{\Sigma_{0}}
in Construction
5.1
is induced by the normalization in
\mathcal{S}^{\Sigma_{0}}_{K_{0}}
of
\mathcal{S}_{K^{\ddagger}}^{\mathrm{min}}
; in other words,
\mathcal{S}_{K_{0}}^{\mathrm{min}}
is the normalization in
\mathcal{S}_{K_{0}}^{\Sigma_{0}}
of
\mathcal{S}_{K^{\ddagger}}^{\mathrm{min}}
: To see this, denote this normalization by
\mathcal{S}^{\mathrm{min}}_{1}
. The normalization induces a Stein factorization of
\oint_{K^{\ddagger}}^{\Sigma^{\ddagger}}\circ\iota
,

\mathcal{S}_{K_{0}}^{\Sigma_{0}}\xrightarrow{\pi_{2}}\mathcal{S}_{1}^{\mathrm{min}}\xrightarrow{\pi_{1}}\mathcal{S}_{K^{\ddagger}}^{\mathrm{min}}.

Since
\pi_{2}^{*}\pi_{1}^{*}\omega_{K^{\ddagger}}^{\mathrm{min}}\cong\iota^{*}\oint_{K^{\ddagger}}^{\Sigma^{\ddagger},*}\omega_{K^{\ddagger}}^{\mathrm{min}}
, we have

\mathrm{Proj}\bigoplus_{k=0}^{\infty}\Gamma(\mathcal{S}_{K_{0}}^{\Sigma_{0}},\omega_{K_{0}}^{\mathrm{tor},\otimes k})\cong\mathrm{Proj}\bigoplus_{k=0}^{\infty}\Gamma(\mathcal{S}_{1}^{\mathrm{min}},(\pi_{1}^{*}\omega_{K^{\ddagger}}^{\mathrm{min}})^{\otimes k})\cong\mathcal{S}_{1}^{\mathrm{min}}.

The first isomorphism is due to the projection formula and the second one holds because
\pi_{1}
is finite and therefore
\pi_{1}^{*}\omega_{K^{\ddagger}}^{\mathrm{min}}
is ample.

Then the normalization in
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{0}}
of
\mathcal{S}_{K^{\ddagger}}^{\mathrm{min}}
has a finite birational morphism to
\mathcal{S}_{K_{0}}^{\mathrm{min}}
and is isomorphic to
\mathcal{S}_{K_{0}}^{\mathrm{min}}
by Zariski’s main theorem. Similarly, by functoriality of relative normalizations and Zariski’s main theorem,
\mathcal{S}_{K_{0}}^{\mathrm{min}}
is also isomorphic to the normalization in
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{0}}^{\Sigma_{0}}
or
\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{min}}_{K_{0}}
of
\mathcal{S}_{K^{\ddagger}}^{\mathrm{min}}
.
∎

5.1.3.

Now we resume with the notation and conventions in §
1.4.4
and §
4.2
.
Fix any
\gamma\in G^{\mathrm{ad}}_{0}(\mathbb{Q})_{1}
. Recall that by Lemma
4.14
(cf. Lemma
4.10
), there is a morphism
\gamma:\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{0}^{\alpha}},h}\to\mathcal{S}_{{}^{\gamma}{K_{\Phi_{0}^{\alpha}},h}}=\mathcal{S}_{K_{{}^{\gamma}{\Phi_{0}^{\alpha}}},h}
extending the conjugation
\gamma:\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi_{0}^{\alpha}},h}\to\mathrm{Sh}_{{}^{\gamma}{K_{\Phi_{0}^{\alpha}},h}}
defined over the generic fiber.

As in §
4.2.3
, we choose a Hodge embedding
\iota^{\gamma}:(G_{0},X_{0})\hookrightarrow(G^{{\ddagger},\gamma},X^{{\ddagger},\gamma})
. Define
\mathcal{S}_{{}^{\gamma}{K_{0}}}^{\mathrm{min}}:=\mathcal{S}_{{}^{\gamma}{K_{0}}}^{\mathrm{min}}(G_{0},X_{0})
to be the normalization in
\mathrm{Sh}_{{}^{\gamma}{K_{0}}}(G_{0},X_{0})
of
\mathcal{S}_{K^{{\ddagger},\gamma}}(G^{{\ddagger},\gamma},X^{{\ddagger},\gamma})
(see the notation there).

Lemma 5.5
.

The morphisms
\gamma:\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{0}^{\alpha}},h}\to\mathcal{S}_{{}^{\gamma}{K_{\Phi_{0}^{\alpha}},h}}
for integral models of Hodge-type Shimura varieties induce morphisms
\gamma:\mathcal{Z}_{[\Phi_{0}^{\alpha}],K_{0}^{\alpha}}\to\mathcal{Z}_{[{}^{\gamma}{\Phi_{0}^{\alpha}}],{}^{\gamma}{K_{0}^{\alpha}}}
between strata of
\mathcal{S}_{K_{0}^{\alpha}}^{\mathrm{min}}
and
\mathcal{S}_{{}^{\gamma}{K_{0}^{\alpha}}}^{\mathrm{min}}
. These morphisms between strata and the morphism
\gamma:\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{0}}^{\mathrm{min}}\to\mathrm{Sh}_{{}^{\gamma}{K_{0}}}^{\mathrm{min}}
induced by conjugation of
\gamma
(see
[
64
, 12.3]
) together (uniquely) induce a morphism between integral models of minimal compactifications
\gamma:\mathcal{S}_{K_{0}}^{\mathrm{min}}\to\mathcal{S}_{{}^{\gamma}{K_{0}}}^{\mathrm{min}}
. In particular, the construction of
\mathcal{S}_{K_{0}}^{\mathrm{min}}
and
\mathcal{S}_{{}^{\gamma}{K_{0}}}^{\mathrm{min}}
is independent of the choice of Hodge embedding as in (
\mathrm{STB}_{n}
).

Proof.

For the first sentence, we can use
[
50
, Thm. 5.2.11(4)]
and check that
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi_{0}^{\alpha}},h}\to\Delta_{{}^{\gamma}{\Phi_{0}^{\alpha}},{}^{\gamma}{K_{0}}}\backslash\mathrm{Sh}_{{}^{\gamma}{K_{\Phi_{0}^{\alpha}}},h}
factors through
\Delta_{\Phi_{0}^{\alpha},K_{0}}\backslash\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi_{0}^{\alpha}},h}
and that
\gamma\Delta_{\Phi_{0}^{\alpha},K}\gamma^{-1}=\Delta_{{}^{\gamma}{\Phi_{0}^{\alpha}},{}^{\gamma}{K}}
.
The second sentence follows from
[
50
, Lem. A.3.4]
(note that the proof of
loc. cit.
only used the condition that there are morphisms between strata that are compatible with a morphism over the generic fiber for the whole space).
The third sentence also follows from
[
50
, Lem. A.3.4]
and Lemma
4.14
.
∎

5.2.
Construction of minimal compactifications

Our construction for minimal compactifications is similar to the method in §
4.2
and §
4.3
for toroidal compactifications. We will freely use the notation there.

5.2.1.

We first begin with the construction of
\mathcal{S}_{K}^{\mathrm{min}}(G,X_{b})
.

Construction 5.6
.

Since
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{0}^{\alpha}}(G_{0},X_{0})
is open dense in
\mathcal{S}^{\mathrm{min}}_{K_{0}^{\alpha}}(G_{0},X_{0})
, as Lemma
4.17
,
\Delta_{{}^{\alpha}{K}}(G_{0},G)
acts on
\mathcal{S}^{\mathrm{min}}_{K_{0}^{\alpha}}(G_{0},X_{0})
through a finite group.
Let

(5.1)

\mathcal{S}_{K}^{\mathrm{min}}(G,X_{b}):=\coprod_{\alpha\in I_{G/G_{0}}}\mathcal{S}_{K_{0}^{\alpha}}^{\mathrm{min}}(G_{0},X_{0})/\Delta_{{}^{\alpha}{K}}(G_{0},G).

The existence of
\Delta_{{}^{\alpha}{K}}(G_{0},G)
-action on
\mathcal{S}_{K_{0}^{\alpha}}^{\mathrm{min}}(G_{0},X_{0})
follows from Lemma
5.5
above and
[
50
, Prop. 5.2.13]
.
Since
\mathrm{Sh}_{K}(G,X_{b})=\coprod_{\alpha\in I_{G/G_{0}}}\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{0}^{\alpha}}(G_{0},X_{0})/\Delta_{{}^{\alpha}{K}}(G_{0},G)
and since the finite surjective morphism
\coprod_{\alpha\in I_{G/G_{0}}}\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{min}}_{K_{0}^{\alpha}}(G_{0},X_{0})\to\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{min}}_{K}(G,X_{b})
factors through a finite surjective morphism

\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{min}}_{K_{0}^{\alpha}}(G_{0},X_{0})/\Delta_{{}^{\alpha}{K}}(G_{0},G)\to\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{min}}_{K}(G,X_{b}),

the generic fiber of
\mathcal{S}_{K}^{\mathrm{min}}(G,X_{b})
is
\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{min}}_{K}(G,X_{b})
by Zariski’s main theorem.
By construction and
[
38
, Ch. 4, Prop. 1.5]
,
\mathcal{S}_{K}^{\mathrm{min}}(G,X_{b})
is a normal scheme which is flat and projective over
\mathcal{O}^{\prime}_{(v)}
.
\square

5.2.2.

As in §
4.3
, the subscript
\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}
denotes normalized base change to
\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}
.
The diagram formed by taking quotient of
\Delta_{{}^{\alpha}{K}}(G_{0},G)

(5.2)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\coprod_{I_{G/G_{0}}}\mathcal{S}^{\Sigma_{0}^{\alpha}}_{K_{0}^{\alpha},\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\coprod_{I_{G/G_{0}}}\mathcal{S}^{\mathrm{min}}_{K_{0}^{\alpha},\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{S}^{\Sigma}_{K,\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{S}^{\mathrm{min}}_{K,\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi^{b}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\coprod\oint_{K_{0}^{\alpha},\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}^{\Sigma_{0}^{\alpha}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi^{b}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\oint_{K,\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}^{\Sigma}}

induces a morphism
\oint^{\Sigma}_{K,\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}:\mathcal{S}^{\Sigma}_{K,\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}\to\mathcal{S}^{\mathrm{min}}_{K,\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}
. (Again we can check the actions of
\Delta_{{}^{\alpha}{K}}(G_{0},G)
on both source and target in the first row are compatible as they all contain a dense subscheme
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{0}^{\alpha}}
.) This morphism is proper and surjective since both source and target are proper over
\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}
and since all solid arrows in (
5.2
) are surjective.

Lemma 5.7
.

The normalization of
\mathcal{S}^{\mathrm{min}}_{K,\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}
in
\mathcal{S}^{\Sigma}_{K,\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}
is
\mathcal{S}^{\mathrm{min}}_{K,\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}
itself. The morphism
\oint_{K,\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}^{\Sigma}
has geometrically connected fibers.

Proof.

Let
\mathcal{S}^{\prime}
be the normalization of
\mathcal{S}^{\mathrm{min}}_{K,\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}
in
\mathcal{S}^{\Sigma}_{K,\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}
. Taking Stein factorization commutes with flat base change; combing with the discussion in the first prargraph of §
5.1.2
, we check over generic fiber that the induced finite map
\mathcal{S}^{\prime}\to\mathcal{S}^{\mathrm{min}}_{K,\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}
is birational. We then have the conclusion by Zariski’s main theorem and
[
71
,
Thm. 0A1B
]
.
∎

5.2.3.

Fix any index
\alpha\in I_{G/G_{0}}
. Suppose that
(\Phi_{0}^{\alpha},\sigma_{0}^{\alpha})
maps to
(\Phi,\sigma)
as before.

Denote by
\oint_{[(\Phi_{0}^{\alpha},\sigma_{0}^{\alpha})],K_{0}^{\alpha}}
the restriction
\oint_{K_{0}^{\alpha}}^{\Sigma_{0}^{\alpha}}|_{\mathcal{Z}_{[(\Phi_{0}^{\alpha},\sigma_{0}^{\alpha})],K_{0}^{\alpha}}}
. Similar conventions will be applied to restrictions to other strata or their (normalized) base changes.

By Theorem
5.2
and (
5.2
), the image of
\pi^{b,-1}(\mathcal{Z}_{[ZP^{b}(\Phi,\sigma)],K,\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}})=

\coprod_{\alpha\in I_{G/G_{0}}}\coprod_{\pi^{b}(g_{0}^{\alpha})\alpha\sim g^{b}}(\Delta_{{}^{\alpha}{K}}(G_{0},G)\mathcal{Z}_{[(\Phi_{0}^{\alpha},\sigma_{0}^{\alpha})],K_{0}^{\alpha},\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}})

under
\coprod\oint^{\Sigma_{0}^{\alpha}}_{K_{0}^{\alpha},\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}
is

\coprod_{\alpha\in I_{G/G_{0}}}\coprod_{\pi^{b}(g_{0}^{\alpha})\alpha\sim g^{b}}(\Delta_{{}^{\alpha}{K}}(G_{0},G)\mathcal{Z}_{[\Phi_{0}^{\alpha}],K_{0}^{\alpha},\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}).

Construction 5.8
.

From the paragraph above, it makes sense to define

\mathcal{Z}_{[ZP^{b}(\Phi)],K,\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}\cong\mathcal{Z}_{[ZP^{a}(\Phi)],K,\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}:=\coprod_{\alpha\in I_{G/G_{0}}}\coprod_{\pi^{b}(g_{0}^{\alpha})\alpha\sim g^{b}}(\Delta_{{}^{\alpha}{K}}(G_{0},G)\mathcal{Z}_{[\Phi_{0}^{\alpha}],K_{0}^{\alpha},\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}})/\Delta_{{}^{\alpha}{K}}(G_{0},G).

Define
\oint_{[ZP^{b}(\Phi,\sigma)],K,\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}
to be the projection induced by the restriction
\oint_{[(\Phi_{0}^{\alpha},\sigma_{0}^{\alpha})],K_{0}^{\alpha},\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}
.

Lemma 5.9
.

The following statements about
\mathcal{Z}_{[ZP^{b}(\Phi)],K,\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}
are true:

(1)

The generic fiber of the scheme above is
\mathrm{Z}_{[ZP^{a}(\Phi)],K,E^{\prime}_{K_{Z}}}\cong\mathrm{Z}_{[ZP^{b}(\Phi)],K,E^{\prime}_{K_{Z}}}
.

(2)

It is a locally closed normal flat subscheme of
\mathcal{S}_{K,\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}^{\mathrm{min}}
fitting into the commutative diagram

(5.3)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{Z}_{[(\Phi_{0}^{\alpha},\sigma_{0}^{\alpha})],K_{0}^{\alpha},\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{Z}_{[\Phi_{0}^{\alpha}],K_{0}^{\alpha},\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{S}^{\Sigma_{0}^{\alpha}}_{K_{0}^{\alpha},\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{S}_{K_{0}^{\alpha},\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}^{\mathrm{min}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{Z}_{[ZP^{b}(\Phi,\sigma)],K,\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{Z}_{[ZP^{b}(\Phi)],K,\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{S}_{K,\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}^{\Sigma}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{S}_{K,\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}^{\mathrm{min}}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\oint_{[(\Phi_{0}^{\alpha},\sigma_{0}^{\alpha})],K_{0}^{\alpha},\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\oint^{\Sigma_{0}^{\alpha}}_{K_{0}^{\alpha},\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\oint_{[ZP^{b}(\Phi,\sigma)],K,\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}}
Lem.
4.21
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\oint_{K,\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}^{\Sigma}}

Proof.

The first statement follows from Proposition
4.19
and Corollary
1.44
. The scheme
\mathcal{Z}_{[ZP^{b}(\Phi)],K,\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}
is normal and is flat over
\mathcal{O}^{\prime}_{(v)}
by construction. The bottom square is induced by quotient construction, so (
5.3
) is commutative. We now show
j
is an embedding. Denote the image of
j
by
[\mathcal{Z}_{\Phi}]
; it is a locally closed subscheme by induction on the dimension of strata. Moreover,
j:\mathcal{Z}_{[ZP^{b}(\Phi)],K,\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}\to[\mathcal{Z}_{\Phi}]
is finite, surjective and a bijection on geometric points by construction.
From
[
50
, Cor. 5.2.8]
, we see that for any geometric point
\bar{y}
on
\mathcal{Z}_{[\Phi_{0}^{\alpha}],K_{0}^{\alpha},\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}
, there is a structural morphism

{(\mathcal{S}^{\mathrm{min}}_{K_{0}^{\alpha},\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}})}{}^{\wedge{\ }}_{\bar{y}}\to{(\mathcal{Z}_{[\Phi_{0}^{\alpha}],K_{0}^{\alpha},\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}})}{}^{\wedge{\ }}_{\bar{y}}

whose pre-composition with the morphism
{(\mathcal{Z}_{[\Phi_{0}^{\alpha}],K_{0}^{\alpha},\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}})}{}^{\wedge{\ }}_{\bar{y}}\to{(\mathcal{S}^{\mathrm{min}}_{K_{0}^{\alpha},\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}})}{}^{\wedge{\ }}_{\bar{y}}
induced by the embedding
\mathcal{Z}_{[\Phi_{0}^{\alpha}],K_{0}^{\alpha},\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}\to\mathcal{S}^{\mathrm{min}}_{K_{0}^{\alpha},\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}
is an identity.

For any geometric point
\bar{x}
on
\mathcal{Z}_{[ZP^{b}(\Phi)],K,\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}
mapping to
[\bar{x}]
on
[\mathcal{Z}_{\Phi}]
, we have a sequence (cf. Lemma
4.21
)

{(\mathcal{Z}_{[ZP^{b}(\Phi)],K,\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}})}{}^{\wedge{\ }}_{\bar{x}}\to{(\mathcal{S}^{\mathrm{min}}_{K,\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}})}{}^{\wedge{\ }}_{[\bar{x}]}\to{(\mathcal{Z}_{[ZP^{b}(\Phi)],K,\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}})}{}^{\wedge{\ }}_{\bar{x}}

who factors through
{([\mathcal{Z}_{\Phi}])}{}^{\wedge{\ }}_{[\bar{x}]}
with composition an identity. This forces
j
to be an embedding.
∎

From the proof above, we have

Lemma 5.10
.

For any
\bar{x}\in\mathcal{Z}_{[ZP^{b}(\Phi,\sigma)],K,\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}(\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{p})
, the homomorphism

\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathcal{S}^{\mathrm{min}}_{K,\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}},\bar{x}}\to\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathcal{Z}_{[ZP^{b}(\Phi)],K,\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}},\bar{x}}

between complete local rings induced by the embedding
\mathcal{Z}_{[ZP^{b}(\Phi,\sigma)],K,\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{S}^{\mathrm{min}}_{K,\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}
admits a section

\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathcal{Z}_{[ZP^{b}(\Phi)],K,\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}},\bar{x}}\to\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathcal{S}^{\mathrm{min}}_{K,\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}},\bar{x}}

such that the pre-composition of the homomorphism with the section is an identity.

Recall that we constructed

\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}^{b},h}:=\coprod_{\beta\in I_{\Phi}}\gamma_{\beta}\cdot\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{\beta}},h}/\Delta_{K^{\beta}}(P_{0},ZP_{\Phi})

in Construction
4.23
.

Lemma 5.11
.

There is an isomorphism
\mathcal{Z}_{[ZP^{b}(\Phi)],K,\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}\cong\Delta_{\Phi,K}^{ZP}\backslash\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}^{b}_{\Phi},h,\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}
.

Proof.

This follows from the same proof as Proposition
4.32
.
∎

Then
\oint_{[ZP^{b}(\Phi,\sigma)],K,\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}
is identified with the canonical projection
\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}^{b}_{\Phi},\sigma,\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}\to\Delta^{ZP}_{\Phi,K}\backslash\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}^{b}_{\Phi},h,\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}
by density and the corresponding result in characteristic zero theory.

5.2.4.

Since
\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}^{a},h,\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}\cong\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}^{b},h,\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}
,
\mathcal{Z}_{[ZP^{a}(\Phi)],K,\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}\cong\mathcal{Z}_{[ZP^{b}(\Phi)],K,\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}
and
\mathcal{S}^{\Sigma}_{K}(G,X_{a})_{\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}\cong\mathcal{S}^{\Sigma}_{K}(G,X_{b})_{\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}
,
let
\mathcal{S}^{\mathrm{min}}_{K}(G,X_{a})_{\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}:=\mathcal{S}^{\mathrm{min}}_{K}(G,X_{b})_{\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}
.

From the Galois action of
\mathrm{Gal}(E^{\prime}_{K_{Z}}/E^{\prime})
on strata with subscript
b
, we can obtain the action of it for corresponding strata with subscript
a
as follows:
Let
r_{b}:\mathrm{Gal}(E^{\prime}_{K_{Z}}/E^{\prime})\times\mathcal{Z}_{[ZP^{b}(\Phi)],K,\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}\to\mathcal{Z}_{[ZP^{b}(\Phi)],K,\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}
be the action of
\mathrm{Gal}(E^{\prime}_{K_{Z}}/E^{\prime})
on
\mathcal{Z}_{[ZP^{b}(\Phi)],K,\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}
which determines the descent datum of
\mathrm{Z}_{[ZP^{b}(\Phi)],K,E^{\prime}_{K_{Z}}}
from
E^{\prime}_{K_{Z}}
to
E^{\prime}
. Define
r_{a}:\mathrm{Gal}(E^{\prime}_{K_{Z}}/E^{\prime})\times\mathcal{Z}_{[ZP^{b}(\Phi)],K,\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}\to\mathcal{Z}_{[ZP^{a}(\Phi)],K,\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}
by sending each
\sigma\in\mathrm{Gal}(E^{\prime}_{K_{Z}}/E^{\prime})
to
r_{a}(\sigma):=r_{E^{\prime},{K_{Z,p}}}(Z,\{c\})(\sigma)\circ r_{b}(\sigma)=r_{b}(\sigma)\circ r_{E^{\prime},{K_{Z,p}}}(Z,\{c\})(\sigma)
.
Note that we can do this since the action of
Z(\mathbb{A}_{f})
is well defined by Proposition
4.24
and Lemma
5.11
. By
[
50
, Lem. A.3.4]
, there is an action of
Z(\mathbb{A}_{f})
on
\mathcal{S}^{\mathrm{min}}_{K,\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}
extending the Hecke action on
\mathrm{Sh}_{K}^{\mathrm{min}}
and the strata
\mathcal{Z}_{[ZP^{b}(\Phi)],K,\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}
. Note that, to run the proof in

[
50
, Lem. A.3.4]
, one only needs the condition that there are morphisms between strata that are compatible with a morphism over the generic fiber for the whole
\mathrm{Sh}_{K}^{\mathrm{min}}
.

We now do the quotient of
\mathcal{S}^{\mathrm{min}}_{K,\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}
and
\mathcal{Z}_{[ZP^{b}(\Phi)],K,\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}
by
\mathrm{Gal}(E^{\prime}_{K_{Z}}/E^{\prime})
via
r_{a}
.

Construction 5.12
.

Let
\mathcal{S}^{\mathrm{min}}_{K}(G,X_{a})
(resp.
\mathcal{Z}_{[ZP^{a}(\Phi)],K}
) be the quotient of
\mathcal{S}^{\mathrm{min}}_{K,\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}
(resp.
\mathcal{Z}_{[ZP^{b}(\Phi)],K,\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}
) by
\mathrm{Gal}(E^{\prime}_{K_{Z}}/E^{\prime})
via
r_{a}
. Then
\mathcal{S}_{K}^{\mathrm{min}}(G,X_{a})
is normal and is projective (see
[
38
, Ch. 4, Prop. 1.5]
) and flat over
\mathcal{O}^{\prime}_{(v)}
, and
\mathcal{Z}_{[ZP^{a}(\Phi)],K}
is normal and is flat over
\mathcal{O}^{\prime}_{(v)}
.

Proposition 5.13
.

Taking the quotient by
\mathrm{Gal}(E^{\prime}_{K_{Z}}/E^{\prime})
via
r_{a}
induces a proper morphism
\oint_{K}^{\Sigma}:\mathcal{S}_{K}^{\Sigma}(G,X_{a})\to\mathcal{S}^{\mathrm{min}}_{K}(G,X_{a})
with geometrically connected fibers.
The normal scheme
\mathcal{Z}_{[ZP^{a}(\Phi)],K}
is a locally closed subscheme of
\mathcal{S}^{\mathrm{min}}_{K}(G,X_{a})
.

The restriction
\oint_{[ZP^{a}(\Phi)],K}
of
\oint_{K}^{\Sigma}
to
\mathcal{Z}_{[ZP^{a}(\Phi,\sigma)],K}
is isomorphic to the natural projection
\mathcal{Z}_{[ZP^{a}(\Phi,\sigma)],K}\cong\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}^{a},\sigma}\to\Delta_{\Phi,K}^{ZP}\backslash\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi},h}\cong\mathcal{Z}_{[ZP^{a}(\Phi)],K}
.

Proof.

Since this is still doing a quotient, all arguments will be similar to what we have seen. To show
\oint^{\Sigma}_{K}
has geometrically connected fibers, we let
\mathcal{S}^{\prime\prime}
be the normalization in
\mathcal{S}^{\Sigma}_{K}(G,X_{a})
of
\mathcal{S}_{K}^{\mathrm{min}}(G,X_{a})
. But the induced morphism
\mathcal{S}^{\prime\prime}\to\mathcal{S}_{K}^{\mathrm{min}}(G,X_{a})
between normal schemes is finite and birational. We have the conclusion by Zariski’s main theorem and
[
71
,
Thm. 0A1B
]
.

The quotient process induces a quasi-finite birational morphism
j:\mathcal{Z}_{[ZP^{a}(\Phi)],K}\to\mathcal{S}^{\mathrm{min}}_{K}(G,X_{a})
which is a bijection over geometric points onto its image
[\mathcal{Z}^{\prime\prime}]
.
By induction on the dimension of strata, this image
[\mathcal{Z}^{\prime\prime}]
is locally closed in
\mathcal{S}^{\mathrm{min}}_{K}(G,X_{a})
. To show that
j
is an embedding, we again reduce the question to studying complete local rings at geometric points. By Lemma
5.10
,
for any geometric point
\bar{z}\in\mathcal{Z}_{[ZP^{a}(\Phi)],K}(\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{p})
mapping to
[\bar{z}]\in\mathcal{S}^{\mathrm{min}}_{K}(G,X_{a})(\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{p})
, the homomorphism between complete local rings induced by
j

\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathcal{S}^{\mathrm{min}}_{K}(G,X_{a}),[\bar{z}]}\cong(\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathcal{S}^{\mathrm{min}}_{K,\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}},[\bar{z}]})^{\mathrm{Gal}(E^{\prime}_{K_{Z}}/E^{\prime})}\to\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathcal{Z}_{[ZP^{a}(\Phi)],K},\bar{z}}

admits a section
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathcal{Z}_{[ZP^{a}(\Phi)],K,\bar{z}}}\to\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathcal{S}^{\mathrm{min}}_{K}(G,X_{a}),[\bar{z}]}
.
This forces
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathcal{Z}_{[ZP^{a}(\Phi)],K},\bar{z}}\to\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{[\mathcal{Z}^{\prime\prime}],[\bar{z}]}
to be an isomorphism. Here
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathcal{S}^{\mathrm{min}}_{K,\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}},[\bar{z}]}
denotes the complete semi-local ring at the inverse image of
[\bar{z}]
under the quotient map.

The second paragraph follows from the following diagram, which is commutative since it is so over the generic fiber:

(5.4)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{Z}_{[ZP^{a}(\Phi,\sigma)],K,\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{Z}_{[ZP^{a}(\Phi)],K,\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{S}^{\Sigma}_{K,\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{S}_{K,\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}^{\mathrm{min}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{Z}_{[ZP^{a}(\Phi,\sigma)],K}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{Z}_{[ZP^{a}(\Phi)],K}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{S}_{K}^{\Sigma}(G,X_{a})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{S}_{K}^{\mathrm{min}}(G,X_{a}).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\oint_{[ZP^{a}(\Phi,\sigma)],K,\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\oint^{\Sigma}_{K,\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\oint_{[ZP^{a}(\Phi,\sigma)],K}}
Lem.
4.49
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\oint_{K}^{\Sigma}}

∎

5.2.5.

Recall that by Corollary
1.37
and our construction,
K
is chosen such that
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{2}}^{\mathrm{min}}(G_{2},X_{2})\to\mathrm{Sh}_{K}^{\mathrm{min}}(G,X_{a})
and
\mathrm{Z}_{[\Phi_{2}],K_{2}}\to\mathrm{Z}_{[\Phi],K}
are open and closed embeddings for each
\Phi_{2}\mapsto\Phi
.

Construction 5.14
.

As the final step, construct
\mathcal{S}_{K_{2}}^{\mathrm{min}}(G_{2},X_{2})
as the normalization of
\mathcal{S}_{K}^{\mathrm{min}}(G,X_{a})
in
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{2}}^{\mathrm{min}}
, and construct
\mathcal{Z}_{[\Phi_{2}],K_{2}}
as the normalization of
\mathcal{Z}_{[ZP^{a}(\Phi)],K}
in
\mathrm{Z}_{[\Phi_{2}],K_{2}}
.

5.3.
Main theorem on minimal compactifications

Let us now summarize the main theorem:

Theorem 5.15
.

With the conventions in Theorem
4.39
, there is a normal scheme
\mathcal{S}_{K_{2}}^{\mathrm{min}}
that is projective and flat over
\mathcal{O}_{2}
, which satisfies the following properties:

(
5.15
.1)

The scheme
\mathcal{S}_{K_{2}}
is an open dense subscheme of
\mathcal{S}^{\mathrm{min}}_{K_{2}}
with a natural open embedding
J^{\mathrm{min}}:\mathcal{S}_{K_{2}}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{S}_{K_{2}}^{\mathrm{min}}
.

(
5.15
.2)

The compactification
\mathcal{S}_{K_{2}}^{\mathrm{min}}
has a stratification of locally closed normal subschemes

\mathcal{S}_{K_{2}}^{\mathrm{min}}=\coprod_{[\Phi_{2}]\in\mathrm{Cusp}_{K_{2}}(G_{2},X_{2})}\mathcal{Z}_{[\Phi_{2}],K_{2}},

such that, for each
[\Phi_{2}]
,
\mathcal{Z}_{[\Phi_{2}],K_{2}}
is flat over
\mathcal{O}_{2}
extending the stratum
\mathrm{Z}_{[\Phi_{2}],K_{2}}
defined in Pink’s theory. The closure
\overline{\mathcal{Z}}_{[\Phi_{2}],K_{2}}
of
\mathcal{Z}_{[\Phi_{2}],K_{2}}
in
\mathcal{S}_{K_{2}}^{\mathrm{min}}
satisfies
\overline{\mathcal{Z}}_{[\Phi_{2}],K_{2}}=\coprod_{[\Phi_{2}^{\prime}]\preceq[\Phi_{2}]}\mathcal{Z}_{[\Phi_{2}^{\prime}],K_{2}}
.

(
5.15
.3)

There is a proper surjective morphism

\oint_{K_{2}}^{\Sigma_{2}}:\mathcal{S}_{K_{2}}^{\Sigma_{2}}\longrightarrow\mathcal{S}^{\mathrm{min}}_{K_{2}}

with geometrically connected fibers such that
\oint_{K_{2}}\circ J^{\Sigma_{2}}=J^{\mathrm{min}}
. The morphism
\oint_{K_{2}}^{\Sigma_{2}}
is compatible with stratifications on the source and the target in the sense that

\oint^{\Sigma_{2},-1}_{K_{2}}(\mathcal{Z}_{[\Phi_{2}],K_{2}})=\coprod_{\Upsilon_{2}\in\mathrm{Cusp}_{K_{2}}(G_{2},X_{2},\Sigma_{2})\mapsto[\Phi_{2}]}\mathcal{Z}_{\Upsilon_{2},K_{2}}.

(
5.15
.4)

For each
[\Phi_{2}]
,
\mathcal{Z}_{[\Phi_{2}],K_{2}}
is isomorphic to
\Delta_{\Phi_{2},K_{2}}\backslash\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{2}},h}
. The restriction of
\oint_{K_{2}}^{\Sigma_{2}}
to any stratum
\mathcal{Z}_{\Upsilon_{2},K_{2}}
of
\mathcal{S}_{K_{2}}^{\Sigma_{2}}
labeled by
\Upsilon_{2}=[(\Phi_{2},\sigma_{2})]
is the natural projection (see (
4.22
) for conventions)

\mathcal{Z}_{\Upsilon,K_{2}}\cong\Delta^{\circ}_{\Phi_{2},K_{2}}\backslash\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{2}},\sigma_{2}}\xrightarrow{\mathbf{p}_{2}\circ\mathbf{p}_{1}}\Delta_{\Phi_{2},K_{2}}^{\circ}\backslash\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{2}},h}\to\Delta_{\Phi_{2},K_{2}}\backslash\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{2}},h}\cong\mathcal{Z}_{[\Phi_{2}],K_{2}}.

Proof.

It suffices to show all statements above over
\mathcal{O}^{\prime}_{(v)}
.
Let
\mathcal{Z}_{[\Phi],K}
be the normalization of
\mathcal{Z}_{[ZP^{a}(\Phi)],K}
in
\mathrm{Z}_{[\Phi],K}
. By Corollary
1.44
,
\mathcal{Z}_{[\Phi],K}
is an open and closed subscheme of
\mathcal{Z}_{[ZP^{a}(\Phi)],K}
. By construction,
\mathcal{Z}_{[\Phi_{2}],K_{2}}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathcal{Z}_{[\Phi],K}
, and
\mathcal{S}_{K_{2}}^{\mathrm{min}}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{S}_{K}^{\mathrm{min}}
is an open and closed embedding. So
\mathcal{Z}_{[\Phi_{2}],K_{2}}
is a locally closed normal subscheme of
\mathcal{S}_{K_{2}}^{\mathrm{min}}
and is flat over
\mathcal{O}_{(v)}^{\prime}
for each
[\Phi_{2}]
. So
(.1)
is proved.

It follows from Proposition
1.41
, Proposition
5.13
and Zariski’s main theorem that
\Delta_{\Phi_{2},K_{2}}\backslash\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{2}},h}\cong\mathcal{Z}_{[\Phi_{2}],K_{2}}
.

The morphism
\oint_{K_{2}}^{\Sigma_{2}}
and its restriction
\oint_{[(\Phi_{2},\sigma)],K_{2}}
to
\mathcal{Z}_{[\Phi_{2},\sigma_{2}],K_{2}}
is given by the following diagram

(5.5)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{Z}_{[(\Phi_{2},\sigma_{2})],K_{2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{Z}_{[\Phi_{2}],K_{2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{S}^{\Sigma_{2}}_{K_{2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{S}_{K_{2}}^{\mathrm{min}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{Z}_{[(\Phi,\sigma)],K}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{Z}_{[\Phi],K}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{S}_{K}^{\Sigma}(G,X_{a})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{S}_{K}^{\mathrm{min}}(G,X_{a})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\oint_{[(\Phi_{2},\sigma_{2})],K_{2}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\oint^{\Sigma_{2}}_{K_{2}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\oint_{[(\Phi,\sigma)],K}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\oint_{K}^{\Sigma}}

by pulling back the bottom square along
\mathcal{S}_{K_{2}}^{\mathrm{min}}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{S}_{K}^{\mathrm{min}}(G,X_{a})
.
Then
(.3)
and
(.4)
follow.
For
(.2)
, it remains to show the statement about the closure of a stratum. But this follows from
(.3)
and
(.2)
.
∎

5.4.
Pink’s formula

We show Pink’s formula when
K_{2,p}
is an intersection of
n
quasi-parahoric subgroups.

5.4.1.

It is not hard to verify the following statements.

Lemma 5.16
.

Under Assumption (
4.29
), the natural quotient
\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}^{a},h}\to\Delta^{ZP}_{\Phi,K}\backslash\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}^{a},h}
is finite étale.

Proof.

By assumption and Lemma
4.57
, it suffices to prove the statement for
\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}^{b},h}\to\Delta^{ZP}_{\Phi,K}\backslash\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}^{b},h}
. This follows from Part
3
of Lemma
4.25
, together with the fact that

\Delta^{ZP}_{\Phi,K}\backslash\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}^{b},h}\cong\coprod_{\alpha\in I_{G/G_{0}}}\coprod_{\pi(g_{0}^{\alpha})\alpha\sim g^{b}}\Delta_{{}^{g_{0}^{\alpha}g_{\alpha}}{K}}(P_{\Phi_{0}^{\alpha}},G)\backslash\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{0}^{\alpha}},h}

from the proof of Proposition
4.32
(or Lemma
5.11
).
∎

We immediately obtain that

Lemma 5.17

(cf.
[
40
, Assumption 4.3.1]
)
.

Under Assumption (
4.29
),
\overline{\mathcal{S}}^{*}_{K_{\Phi_{2}}}\to\mathcal{S}^{*}_{K_{\Phi_{2}},h}
is an abelian scheme torsor and
\mathcal{S}^{*}_{K_{\Phi_{2}},h}\to\Delta_{\Phi_{2},K_{2}}\backslash\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{2}},h}
is finite étale.

Proof.

This follows from Lemma
5.16
and Proposition
4.59
.
∎

Now we convert the left action of
\Delta_{\Phi_{2},K_{2}}
on
\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{2}},h}
and
\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{2}},h}^{*}
to a right action to match the conventions in
[
65
]
.
Following
[
65
, 3.7.4]
, denote

H_{\Phi_{2},K_{2}}:=g_{\Phi_{2}}K_{2}g_{\Phi_{2}}^{-1}\cap\mathrm{Stab}_{Q_{\Phi_{2}}(\mathbb{Q})}(D_{\Phi_{2},h})P_{\Phi_{2}}(\mathbb{A}_{f}),

H_{\Phi_{2},K_{2}}^{C}:=g_{\Phi_{2}}K_{2}g_{\Phi_{2}}^{-1}\cap\mathrm{Cent}_{Q_{\Phi_{2}}(\mathbb{Q})}(D_{\Phi_{2},h})W_{\Phi_{2}}(\mathbb{A}_{f}),

and

K_{W,\Phi_{2}}:=g_{\Phi_{2}}K_{2}g_{\Phi_{2}}^{-1}\cap W_{\Phi_{2}}(\mathbb{A}_{f}).

Recall that any representative
(h,\gamma)\in P_{\Phi_{2}}(\mathbb{A}_{f})\times\mathrm{Stab}_{Q_{\Phi_{2}}(\mathbb{Q})}(D_{\Phi_{2}})
of
\Delta_{\Phi_{2},K_{2}}
acts on
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi_{2}}}\to\overline{\mathrm{Sh}}_{K_{\Phi_{2}}}\to\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi_{2}},h}
by the left multiplication of
\gamma
, which is equivalent to the left conjugation of
\gamma
followed by the right action of
h
; converting it to a right action means a right conjugation of
\gamma^{-1}
followed by the right action of
h
. For any
(\gamma^{-1},h)\in\mathrm{Stab}_{Q_{\Phi_{2}}(\mathbb{Q})}(D_{\Phi_{2}})\times P_{\Phi_{2}}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
such that
\gamma^{-1}h\in H_{\Phi_{2},K_{2}}
, we define a right action of this pair given by the converted right action we just mentioned. It is easy to verify that for any
p\in P_{\Phi_{2}}(\mathbb{Q})=\mathrm{Stab}_{Q_{\Phi_{2}}(\mathbb{Q})}(D_{\Phi_{2}})\cap P_{\Phi_{2}}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
, the action of
(\gamma^{-1}p^{-1},ph)
is equal to that of
(\gamma^{-1},h)
. Hence, the left action of
\Delta_{\Phi_{2},K_{2}}
is equivalent to the right action of
H_{\Phi_{2},K_{2}}/K_{\Phi_{2}}
.

5.4.2.

Now we state Pink’s formula for general abelian-type Shimura varieties. Note that, since the assumption
[
65
, 3.1.5]
that the connected center of
G_{2}
is an extension of a split
\mathbb{Q}
-torus and a compact-type torus is not true for some abelian type Shimura data that are not of Hodge type, the formulation is slightly different from those in
[
65
]
and
[
40
]
.

For any connected algebraic group
H
over
\mathbb{Q}
, define ??to be the
\mathbb{Q}
-group formed by the quotient of
H
by
Z_{ac}(H)
, where
Z_{ac}(H)
is the minimal
\mathbb{Q}
-subgroup in the connected multiplicative center
Z(H)^{\circ}
of
H
such that
Z(H)^{\circ}/Z_{ac}(H)
has equal
\mathbb{Q}
-rank and
\mathbb{R}
-rank. (Here, the subscript “ac” means “anti-cuspidal”.) As remarked in
[
34
, footnote p.34]
, if we assume that
H
is reductive and splits over a CM field, the definition coincides with that in
[
53
, Ch. III]
.

By
[
20
]
and
[
34
, 1.5.8.2]
, we know that

\mathrm{Gal}(\mathrm{Sh}(G_{2},X_{2})/\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{2}}(G_{2},X_{2}))\cong\frac{K_{2}}{Z_{G_{2}}(\mathbb{Q})^{\overline{\ }}\cap K_{2}},

where
Z_{G_{2}}(\mathbb{Q}){}^{\overline{\ }}\cap K_{2}
is trivial when
K_{2}
is neat and
Z_{G_{2}}
is isogenous to a product of
\mathbb{Q}
-split tori and compact-type tori.

We check if the Hecke actions away from
p
are finite étale:

Lemma 5.18
.

For all cases and for
?=*
or
\emptyset
, consider prime-to-
p
Hecke actions on
\mathcal{S}_{K_{2}}
,
\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{2}}}^{?}
,
\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{K_{\Phi_{2}}}^{?}
and
\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{2}},h}^{?}
. More precisely, for any normal open compact subgroup
K_{2}^{p,\prime}\subset K_{2}^{p}
, denote by
K_{\Phi_{2}}^{p,\prime}:=P_{\Phi_{2}}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})\cap g_{\Phi_{2}}K_{2}^{p,\prime}g_{\Phi_{2}}^{-1}
a normal subgroup of
K_{\Phi_{2}}
. We have the following statements:

(1)

If
K_{2,p}
satisfies Assumption (
4.29
), the natural morphisms
\mathcal{S}_{K_{2,p}K_{2}^{p,\prime}}\to\mathcal{S}_{K_{2,p}K_{2}^{p}}
,
\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{2},p}K_{\Phi_{2}}^{p,\prime}}^{?}\to\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{2},p}K_{\Phi_{2}}^{p}}^{?}
,
\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{K_{\Phi_{2},p}K_{\Phi_{2}}^{p,\prime}}^{?}\to\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{K_{\Phi_{2},p}K_{\Phi_{2}}^{p}}^{?}
and
\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{2},p}K_{\Phi_{2}}^{p,\prime},h}^{?}\to\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{2},p}K_{\Phi_{2}}^{p},h}^{?}

are all finite étale for any neat open compact subgroup
K_{2}^{p}
and
K_{\Phi_{2}}^{p}
.

(2)

In general, the statement above holds for sufficiently small
K_{2}^{p}
and
K_{\Phi_{2}}^{p}
.

Proof.

As in the proof of Lemma
4.48
, by adjusting prime-to-
p
level group, it is enough to show the statement for
?=*
, and this is further reduced to the statement for
\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}^{a}_{\Phi}}
,
\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{\widetilde{K}^{a}_{\Phi}}
and
\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}^{a}_{\Phi},h}
.

Write
\widetilde{K}^{a}_{\Phi}
as
\widetilde{K}_{\Phi,p}^{a}\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}^{a,p}
.
If
K_{2,p}
satisfies (
4.29
), by Lemma
4.57
, the first part follows directly from Lemma
4.25
by reducing to the related statement about
\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}^{b}_{\Phi}}
.

Now we show the second part.
Denote
\widetilde{K}^{+}_{\Phi,p}:=ZP_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\cap g_{\Phi}K_{p}^{+}g_{\Phi}^{-1}
where
K_{p}^{+}
is defined in §
4.3.3
above.
Let
\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}^{a,p,\prime}
be a normal open compact subgroup of
\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}^{a,p}
. Pick any
g^{p}\in\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}^{a,p}
such that the action of it on
\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}^{a,\prime}_{\Phi}}:=\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi,p}^{a}\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}^{a,p,\prime}}
is not free on every geometric point.
By Lemma
4.25
, the Hecke action of
g^{p}\in\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}^{a,p}
on
\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi,p}^{+,a}\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}^{a,p,\prime}}
is not free if and only if its action on
\mathrm{Sh}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi,p}^{+,a}\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}^{a,p,\prime}}
is trivial. Writing
\widetilde{K}_{\Phi,p}^{+,a}\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}^{a,p,\prime}=:\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}^{+,a,\prime}
, the last condition is equivalent to
g^{p}\in\mathrm{Cent}_{ZP_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})}(D_{\Phi})\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}^{+,a,\prime}
.
Replacing
g^{p}
with a multiplication of it by an element in
\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}^{a,p,\prime}
, assume that
g^{p}\in\mathrm{Cent}_{ZP_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})}(D_{\Phi})\widetilde{K}_{\Phi,p}^{+,a}
. Then
g^{p}
comes from an element
c\in\mathrm{Cent}_{ZP_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})}(D_{\Phi})
and
c\in\mathrm{Cent}_{ZP_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})}(D_{\Phi})\cap\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}^{+,a}=\mathrm{Cent}_{ZP_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})}(D_{\Phi})\cap\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}^{a}K^{+}_{Z,p}
. That is, the prime-to-
p
part of
c
, viewed as an
\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p}
-point, is equal to
g^{p}
.

Recall that
\mathrm{Cent}_{ZP_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})}(D_{\Phi})
is multiplicative and recall Chevalley’s theorem
[
11
, Thm. 1]
. We have that

\mathrm{Cent}_{ZP_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})}(D_{\Phi})\cap\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}^{a}K^{+}_{Z,p}=\mathrm{Cent}_{ZP_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})}(D_{\Phi})\cap\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}^{a}

when
\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}^{a,p}
is sufficiently small. Choose such a sufficiently small
\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}^{a,p}
,
we have that the action of
g^{p}
is trivial on all geometric points of
\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}^{a,\prime}_{\Phi}}
.

The proof for
\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{K_{\Phi}^{a}}
and
\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}^{a}_{\Phi},h}
is exactly the same as above.
∎

Fix a prime number
\ell\neq p
. Denote by
\mathcal{G}(K_{2})_{l}
the quotient

\frac{K_{2}}{(K_{2}\cap G_{2}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{l}))(Z_{G_{2}}(\mathbb{Q}){}^{\overline{\ }}\cap K_{2})};

this is a profinite group with a natural homomorphism to
G_{2}^{c}(\mathbb{Q}_{l})
.
Let
\xi
be a continuous representation of
\mathcal{G}(K_{2})_{l}
on a finite-dimensional
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{l}
-vector space
V_{\xi}
. By
[
34
, 1.5.2]
(see also
[
41
, Lem. 3.6]
and
[
25
, III. 2]
), there is a lisse
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{l}
-sheaf
\mathcal{V}_{\xi}
on
\mathcal{S}_{K_{2}}
associated with
V_{\xi}
.

Now assume that
\xi
is an algebraic
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{l}
-representation of
G_{2}^{c}
on a finite-dimensional
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{l}
-vector space
V_{\xi}
, then
\xi
induces a continuous representation
\mathcal{G}(K_{2})_{l}\to G^{c}_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{l})\xrightarrow{\xi}\mathrm{GL}(V_{\xi})
, which we abusively denote by
\xi
. We attach a lisse
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{l}
-sheaf
\mathcal{V}_{\xi}
to
V_{\xi}
as in the last paragraph.

On the other hand,
the group
H^{C}_{\Phi_{2},K_{2}}
is by definition the kernel of the right action of
H_{\Phi_{2},K_{2}}
on
\mathrm{Sh}_{\Phi_{2},h}:=\varprojlim_{K_{2}}\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi_{2}},h}
.
There is a continuous representation of
H_{\Phi_{2},K_{2}}
on
V_{\xi}
induced by

\xi_{\Phi_{2}}:H_{\Phi_{2},K_{2}}\hookrightarrow g_{\Phi_{2}}K_{2}g_{\Phi_{2}}^{-1}\xrightarrow[\sim]{g_{\Phi_{2}}^{-1}(\cdot)g_{\Phi_{2}}}K_{2}\hookrightarrow G_{2}(\mathbb{A}_{f})\to G_{2}^{c}(\mathbb{Q}_{l})\xrightarrow{\xi}\mathrm{GL}(V_{\xi}).

Viewing
V_{\xi}
as a
H_{\Phi_{2},K_{2}}
-representation, it makes sense to define the following object

V^{\natural}_{\xi,\Phi_{2},K_{2}}:=R\mathrm{Inv}(H^{C}_{\Phi_{2},K_{2}},R\mathrm{Inv}(\lie W_{\Phi_{2}}(\mathbb{Q}_{l}),V_{\xi}))

in the derived category of bounded complexes of finite-dimensional
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{l}
-vector spaces with
H_{\Phi_{2},K_{2}}/H^{C}_{\Phi_{2},K_{2}}
-actions. By Part
1
of Lemma
5.18
, we can and will denote by
\mathcal{V}_{\xi,\Phi_{2},K_{2}}
the lisse
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{l}
-sheaf on
\mathcal{Z}_{\Phi_{2},K_{2}}
associated with the
H_{\Phi_{2},K_{2}}/H^{C}_{\Phi_{2},K_{2}}
-representation
V^{\natural}_{\xi,\Phi_{2},K_{2}}
.

From Theorem
5.15
, we have a diagram

\mathcal{S}_{K_{2}}\xhookrightarrow{J^{\mathrm{min}}}\mathcal{S}_{K_{2}}^{\mathrm{min}}\xhookleftarrow{i_{[\Phi_{2}],K_{2}}}\mathcal{Z}_{[\Phi_{2}],K_{2}}.

Proposition 5.19

(Pink; cf.
[
65
, Thm. 4.2.1]
and
[
40
, Thm. 4.3.10]
)
.

Under Assumption (
4.29
), for any algebraic representation
\xi
of
G_{2}^{c}
on a finite-dimensional
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{l}
-vector space
V_{\xi}
, we have

(5.6)

i^{*}_{[\Phi_{2}],K_{2}}RJ_{*}^{\mathrm{min}}\mathcal{V}_{\xi}\cong\mathcal{V}^{\natural}_{\xi,\Phi_{2},K_{2}}.

Proof.

Pink’s formula (
5.6
) can be shown by the same argument in
[
65
, 4.3-4.7]
with the geometric input developed in this article, and we will explain it for the convenience of the readers.

There is a finite extension
F_{l}
of
\mathbb{Q}_{l}
and an
\mathcal{O}_{F_{l}}
-lattice
W_{\xi}\subset V_{\xi}
that is stable under
\mathcal{G}(K_{2})_{l}
such that we can write
V_{\xi}
as
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{l}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{F_{l}}}\varprojlim_{n}W_{\xi}/l^{n}W_{\xi}
and
V_{\xi,n}:=W_{\xi}/l^{n}W_{\xi}
are continuous
\mathbb{Z}/l^{n}\mathbb{Z}
-representations of
\mathcal{G}(K_{2})_{l}
. Let
\mathcal{V}_{\xi,n}
be the
\mathbb{Z}/l^{n}\mathbb{Z}
-sheaf on
\mathcal{S}_{K_{2}}
attached to
V_{\xi,n}
.
Let
\mathcal{Z}_{[\Phi_{2}],K_{2}}^{\mathrm{tor}}
be the preimage
\oint_{K_{2}}^{\Sigma_{2},-1}(\mathcal{Z}_{[\Phi_{2}],K_{2}})
. There is a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{S}_{K_{2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{S}^{\Sigma_{2}}_{K_{2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{Z}^{\mathrm{tor}}_{[\Phi_{2}],K_{2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{S}_{K_{2}}^{\mathrm{min}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{Z}_{[\Phi_{2}],K_{2}}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces J^{\Sigma_{2}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces J^{\mathrm{min}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\oint_{K_{2}}^{\Sigma_{2}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i^{\mathrm{tor}}_{[\Phi_{2}],K_{2}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\oint_{K_{2}}^{\Sigma_{2}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{[\Phi_{2}],K_{2}}}

By Theorem
(.3)
,
\oint_{K_{2}}^{\Sigma_{2}}
is proper. By proper base change, there is a canonical isomorphism

i^{*}_{[\Phi_{2}],K_{2}}RJ_{*}^{\mathrm{min}}\mathcal{V}_{\xi,n}\cong R\oint^{\Sigma_{2}}_{K_{2},*}i^{\mathrm{tor},*}_{[\Phi_{2}],K_{2}}RJ_{*}^{\Sigma_{2}}\mathcal{V}_{\xi,n}.

The argument in
[
65
, Prop. 4.4.3]
still works thanks to
[
65
, 2.7]
and Proposition
4.53
.
We deduce a canonical isomorphism

R\oint^{\Sigma_{2}}_{K_{2},*}i^{\mathrm{tor},*}_{[\Phi_{2}],K_{2}}RJ_{*}^{\Sigma_{2}}\mathcal{V}_{\xi,n}\cong R\oint^{\Sigma_{2}}_{K_{2},*}R\mathrm{Inv}(H_{\Phi_{2},K_{2}},V_{\xi,n}\otimes\varinjlim_{U_{l}}\phi_{U_{l},*}\mathbb{Z}),

where
U_{l}\subset G_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{l})
runs over a cofinal system of sufficiently small open compact subgroups of
G_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{l})
such that
K_{2}^{p}\cap G_{2}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p,l})U_{l}\subset K_{2}^{p}
, and
\phi_{U_{l}}:\mathcal{Z}_{[\Phi_{2}],K_{2,p}K_{2}^{p,l}U_{l}}^{\mathrm{tor}}\to\mathcal{Z}^{\mathrm{tor}}_{[\Phi_{2}],K_{2}}
is the natural transition map.

The
\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{2}}}^{*}\to\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{K_{\Phi_{2}}}^{*}\to\mathcal{S}^{*}_{K_{\Phi_{2}},h}\to\Delta_{\Phi_{2},K_{2}}\backslash\mathcal{S}_{K_{\Phi_{2}},h}
in our convention corresponds to
\Xi\to C\to C^{\mathrm{st}}\to\mathsf{Z}
in the conventions of
[
40
, Sec. 4.3]
; all terms and morphisms in this sequence satisfy
[
40
, Prop. 2.1.2 and Assumption 4.3.1(4)]
by Theorem
4.39
, Theorem
5.15
and Lemma
5.17
.
Note that
[
40
, Lem. 4.3.2]
follows from characteristic zero theory, Lemma
5.17
and
[
21
, I. Prop. 2.7]
. Together with Part
1
of Lemma
5.18
, the argument in
[
65
, 4.6 and 4.7]
works. We deduce that

R\oint^{\Sigma_{2}}_{K_{2},*}R\mathrm{Inv}(H_{\Phi_{2},K_{2}},V_{\xi,n}\otimes\varinjlim_{U_{l}}\phi_{U_{l},*}\mathbb{Z})
is isomorphic to the complex of
\mathbb{Z}/l^{n}\mathbb{Z}
-sheaves on
\mathcal{Z}_{[\Phi_{2}],K_{2}}
in the derived category associated with
R\mathrm{Inv}(H^{C}_{\Phi_{2},K_{2}},V_{\xi,n})
. We now have that
i^{*}_{[\Phi_{2}],K_{2}}RJ_{*}^{\mathrm{min}}\mathcal{V}_{\xi}
is isomorphic to the complex of
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{l}
-sheaves on
\mathcal{Z}_{[\Phi_{2}],K_{2}}
in the derived category associated with
R\mathrm{Inv}(H^{C}_{\Phi_{2},K_{2}},V_{\xi})
. Finally, we obtain (
5.6
) by the proof in
[
65
, 5.2 and 5.3]
.
∎

5.5.
Nearby cycles

This subsection is to generalize
[
41
, Thm. 4.1]
to the abelian-type case. As the readers will see, the proof requires essentially nothing but (a generalization of) the qualitative descriptions of good compactifications stated there.

Remark 5.20
.

Unlike the Case (Ab) in
[
41
]
which only deals with a “crude” construction of minimal compactifications for integral models in
[
33
]
, the proof presented here works for all integral models constructed in this paper without technical assumptions on
G_{2}
and the level at
p
.

Proposition 5.21

(Lan-Stroh; cf.
[
41
, Thm. 4.1 and Cor. 4.6]
)
.

Fix a prime
l\neq p
.
Let
\mathcal{V}
be either a lisse
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{l}
-sheaf associated with an algebraic representation
\xi
of
G^{c}_{2}
on a finite-dimensional
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{l}
-vector space
V_{\xi}
, or a finite
\mathbb{Z}_{l}
-module equipped with an action of an open compact subgroup
G_{2}^{c}(\mathbb{Z}_{l})
of
G_{2}^{c}(\mathbb{Q}_{l})
. Denote by
\eta
(resp.
s
) the generic (resp. special) point of the base ring of the integral model
\mathcal{S}_{K_{2}}
and denote the corresponding geometric point by adding “
\overline{\ }
”.

In all cases (
\mathrm{HS}
), (
\mathrm{STB}_{n}
) and (
\mathrm{DL}
), assume that the projection of
K_{2}
into
G^{c}_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{l})
factors through
G^{c}_{2}(\mathbb{Z}_{l})
. Then there are natural isomorphisms induced by adjunctions that are equivariant under the actions of the absolute Galois group
\mathrm{Gal}(\overline{\eta}/\eta)
:

(5.7)

R\Psi_{\mathcal{S}_{K_{2}}^{\Sigma_{2}}}RJ_{\eta,*}^{\Sigma_{2}}\mathcal{V}\xrightarrow{\sim}RJ_{\overline{s},*}^{\Sigma_{2}}R\Psi_{\mathcal{S}_{K_{2}}}\mathcal{V},

(5.8)

J_{\overline{s},!}^{\Sigma_{2}}R\Psi_{\mathcal{S}_{K_{2}}}\mathcal{V}\xrightarrow{\sim}R\Psi_{\mathcal{S}_{K_{2}}^{\Sigma_{2}}}J_{\eta,!}^{\Sigma_{2}}\mathcal{V},

(5.9)

R\Gamma_{et}(\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{2},\overline{\eta}},\mathcal{V})\xrightarrow{\sim}R\Gamma_{et}(\mathcal{S}_{K_{2},\overline{s}},R\Psi_{\mathcal{S}_{K_{2}}}\mathcal{V}),

and

(5.10)

R\Gamma_{et,c}(\mathcal{S}_{K_{2},\overline{s}},R\Psi_{\mathcal{S}_{K_{2}}}\mathcal{V})\xrightarrow{\sim}R\Gamma_{et,c}(\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{2},\overline{\eta}},\mathcal{V}).

In the equations above, “
R\Psi
” denotes nearby cycles (see
[
15
, Exp. XIII]
).

Proof.

The last two isomorphisms (
5.9
) and (
5.10
) follow from the first two (
5.7
) and (
5.8
) and the proper base change. (Alternatively, when
K_{2,p}
is parahoric, we can choose
\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}
so that the normalized base changes in Corollary
4.55
are base changes by Lemma
4.57
. Then one can also use the Hochschild-Serre spectral sequence and Corollary
4.55
to show (
5.9
) and (
5.10
) as in the proof of
[
41
, Cor. 4.6]
.)

We show the first two isomorphisms. The proof is almost identical to that in
[
41
]
given the established geometric inputs in this paper. We will repeat it and highlight the difference.

It suffices to assume that
\mathcal{V}
is associated with a finite
\mathbb{Z}/l^{n}\mathbb{Z}
-module
\Lambda_{n}
. We first choose a prime-to-
p
level
K^{p,\prime}_{2}
such that
K^{p,\prime}_{2}
acts trivially on
\Lambda_{n}
. By Lemma
5.22
, we can choose suitable
K_{2}^{p,\prime}
to further make the transition maps in Lemma
5.18
(1) finite étale.
Then the argument in
[
41
, Prop. 5.1 and Prop. 5.3]
still works as summarized below.

Denote the base ring by
\mathcal{O}
until the end of this proof. We now fix a point
x
in a stratum
\mathcal{Z}_{\sigma}:=\mathcal{Z}_{[(\Phi_{2},\sigma_{2})],K_{2}}
. For simplicity, write
\sigma:=\sigma_{2}
. We know that
\mathcal{S}_{K_{2}}^{\Sigma_{2}}
is étale locally isomorphic to
C^{\prime}\times_{\mathcal{O}}\mathbf{E}
by Lemma
5.22
below, and that we can reduce the problem to
C^{\prime}\times_{\mathcal{O}}\mathbf{E}
by assuming that
\mathcal{V}:=\mathcal{V}_{E}\boxtimes\underline{\mathbb{Z}/l^{n}\mathbb{Z}}
for an étale
\mathbb{Z}/l^{n}\mathbb{Z}
-module
\mathcal{V}_{E}
over
\mathbf{E}
that is trivialized over
\mathbf{E}^{\prime}
and a constant sheaf
\underline{\mathbb{Z}/l^{n}\mathbb{Z}}
over
C^{\prime}
. (We first have a constant sheaf
\underline{\Lambda_{n}}
on
\mathcal{S}^{*}_{K_{\Phi_{2},p}K_{\Phi_{2}}^{p,\prime}}
and pull it back to
C^{\prime}\times_{\mathcal{O}}\mathbf{E}^{\prime}
, and we then descend it to
C^{\prime}\times_{\mathcal{O}}\mathbf{E}
with the given
(K_{2}^{p}\cap(Z_{G_{2}}\times U_{\Phi_{2}})(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p}))
-action.)

Note that if the coefficient we are considering is already trivial itself, we do not need the reduction step in the last two paragraphs and can directly reduce the problem to the problem over
C\times_{\mathcal{O}}\mathbf{E}
for trivial coefficients.

Now the argument in
[
41
, pp.18-19]
gets through verbatim, and it shows that

R\Psi_{C^{\prime}\times_{\mathcal{O}}\mathbf{E}(\sigma)}Rj_{\eta,*}\mathcal{V}\xrightarrow{\sim}Rj_{\overline{s},*}R\Psi_{C^{\prime}\times_{\mathcal{O}}\mathbf{E}}\mathcal{V}

and that

j_{\overline{s},!}R\Psi_{C^{\prime}\times_{\mathcal{O}}\mathbf{E}}\mathcal{V}\xrightarrow{\sim}R\Psi_{C^{\prime}\times_{\mathcal{O}}\mathbf{E}(\sigma)}j_{\eta,!}\mathcal{V}

for
j:C^{\prime}\times_{\mathcal{O}}\mathbf{E}\hookrightarrow C^{\prime}\times_{\mathcal{O}}\mathbf{E}(\sigma)
.
We now have the desired isomorphisms.
∎

Lemma 5.22
.

Assume that
\mathcal{V}
is associated with a finite
\mathbb{Z}/l^{n}\mathbb{Z}
-module
\Lambda_{n}
. Suppose that a neat open compact subgroup
K^{p,\prime\prime}_{2}\subset K^{p}_{2}
acts trivially on
\Lambda_{n}
via
K^{p}_{2}\to\mathcal{G}(K_{2})_{l}\to G_{2}^{c}(\mathbb{Q}_{l})
.

(1)

We can choose a prime-to-
p
level
K^{p,\prime}_{2}
such that
K^{p,\prime}_{2}
acts trivially on
\Lambda_{n}
and the transition maps in Lemma
5.18
(1) are finite étale.

(2)

Fix any cusp label
[(\Phi_{2},\sigma_{2})]
. Under the choice in the first item, we have the following commutative diagram:

(5.11)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{S}^{*}_{K_{\Phi_{2},p}K_{\Phi_{2}}^{p,\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{S}^{*}_{K_{\Phi_{2},p}K_{\Phi_{2}}^{p}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C^{\prime}:=\overline{\mathcal{S}}^{*}_{K_{\Phi_{2},p}K_{\Phi_{2}}^{p,\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C:=\overline{\mathcal{S}}^{*}_{K_{\Phi_{2},p}K_{\Phi_{2}}^{p}}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{E}^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{E}}

The horizontal maps are finite étale, the vertical maps are torsors under split tori
\mathbf{E}^{\prime}
and
\mathbf{E}
, respectively, and the diagram is equivariant under a finite étale isogeny between the tori
\mathbf{E}^{\prime}\to\mathbf{E}.

Proof.

We adopt the notation in the proof of Lemma
5.18
. We reduce to the case where
