(G_{2},X_{2})=(G,X_{a})
. We see from the proof there that, if we replace
K^{p,\prime}
with
K^{p,\prime}\cdot(Z_{G}(\mathbb{Q})\cap K_{Z,p}^{+}K)^{p}
(where
(-)^{p}
denotes viewing
\mathbb{Q}
-points as
\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p}
-points), then
\mathcal{S}_{K^{\prime}}\to\mathcal{S}_{K}
is finite étale. By the proof of
[
64
, Cor. 4.10]
and since
\mathrm{Cent}_{ZP_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})}(D_{\Phi})\cap\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}^{+,a}
is neat, we have that
\mathrm{Cent}_{ZP_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})}(D_{\Phi})\cap\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}^{+,a}\subset Z_{G}(\mathbb{Q})\cap\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}^{+,a}\subset Z_{G}(\mathbb{Q})\cap K_{Z,p}^{+}K
. From the proof of Lemma
5.18
, the transition maps
\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}^{a,\prime}}\to\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}^{a}}
and
\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}^{a,\prime}}\to\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}^{a}}
are finite étale if we replace
K^{p,\prime}
with
K^{p,\prime}\cdot(Z_{G}(\mathbb{Q})\cap K_{Z,p}^{+}K)^{p}
. Since the action of
K^{p}
on
\Lambda_{n}
factors through
G^{c}(\mathbb{Q}_{l})
and the projection of
Z_{G}(\mathbb{Q})\cap K_{Z,p}^{+}K
to
G^{c}(\mathbb{Q}_{l})
is trivial, we have shown the first item. The second item follows from the first one and Theorem
4.39
.
∎

The corollaries for intersection cohomology now also hold for all cases (
\mathrm{HS}
), (
\mathrm{STB}_{n}
) and (
\mathrm{DL}
) by proper base change and by the proof of
[
42
, Thm. 5.26]
.

Corollary 5.23
.

Let
\mathcal{V}
be a lisse
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{l}
-sheaf, or an
\mathbb{F}_{l}
- or
\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{l}
-sheaf. We have, in all cases, the following natural isomorphisms that are equivariant under the actions of
\mathrm{Gal}(\overline{\eta}/\eta)
:

(5.12)

R\Psi_{\mathcal{S}_{K_{2}}^{\mathrm{min}}}RJ_{\eta,*}^{\mathrm{min}}\mathcal{V}\xrightarrow{\sim}RJ_{\overline{s},*}^{\mathrm{min}}R\Psi_{\mathcal{S}_{K_{2}}}\mathcal{V},

(5.13)

J_{\overline{s},!}^{\mathrm{min}}R\Psi_{\mathcal{S}_{K_{2}}}\mathcal{V}\xrightarrow{\sim}R\Psi_{\mathcal{S}_{K_{2}}^{\mathrm{min}}}J_{\eta,!}^{\mathrm{min}}\mathcal{V},

and

(5.14)

R\Psi_{\mathcal{S}_{K_{2}}^{\mathrm{min}}}J_{,\eta,!*}^{\mathrm{min}}\mathcal{V}[d]\xrightarrow{\sim}J_{\overline{s},!*}^{\mathrm{min}}R\Psi_{\mathcal{S}_{K_{2}}}\mathcal{V}[d].

5.5.1.
Remarks on future projects

Now we have established a good compactification theory in which
\oint_{K_{2}}^{\Sigma_{2}}:\mathcal{S}_{K_{2}}^{\Sigma_{2}}\to\mathcal{S}_{K_{2}}^{\mathrm{min}}
has the desired properties as in
[
42
, Prop. 2.2]
and
[
40
, Prop. 2.1.2]
.
In some future projects, we plan to study some aspects that have not been addressed in this paper.

Remark 5.24
.

In a forthcoming joint work with Shengkai Mao, we will study the extension of
p
-adic shtukas to toroidal compactifications, where a framework generalizing
[
63
]
to show the well-positionedness of many types of strata on the special fibers of Shimura varieties, and to describe the uniqueness and functoriality of integral models of toroidal compactifications is pursued.

Remark 5.25
.

Unlike Hodge-type Shimura varieties, a general abelian-type Shimura variety does not automatically have a “Hodge invertible sheaf” determined by the pullback of some universal abelian variety under a Hodge embedding. Hence, this requires a more systematic treatment which is different from that in
[
50
, Sec. 5]
.

We plan to study the extension of canonical sheaves and “Hodge invertible sheaves” to integral models of minimal compactifications, as well as the construction of Fourier-Jacobi expansions. In addition, we also want to extend the main theorems of this paper to more general setups such as allowing all projective cone decompositions for
(G_{2},X_{2},K_{2})
and constructing toroidal compactifications as normalized blow-ups.

Appendix A
Comparison with PEL-type cusp labels

Let us review Lan’s definition of (PEL-type) cusp labels and its relation, in the case of PEL-type Shimura varieties, with the definition of cusp labels introduced in
[
64
]
and
[
50
]
, and summarized in §
1.1.4
.
See
[
44
]
,
[
43
]
and the appendix of
[
74
]
.

A.1.
PEL-type Shimura varieties

Let us fix a PEL-type
\mathcal{O}
-lattice
\mathcal{D}:=(V_{\mathbb{Z}},\psi_{\mathbb{Z}},h_{0})
as defined in
[
44
, Def. 1.2.1.3]
, where
\mathcal{O}
is an order of a finite semisimple
\mathbb{Q}
-algebra with a positive involution
*
,
V_{\mathbb{Z}}
is a
\mathbb{Z}
-module with
\mathcal{O}
-action,
\psi_{\mathbb{Z}}
is an alternating pairing of
V_{\mathbb{Z}}
such that
\psi_{\mathbb{Z}}(x,by)=\psi_{\mathbb{Z}}(b^{*}x,y)
and
h_{0}
is a Hodge cocharacter
h_{0}:\mathbb{C}\longrightarrow\mathrm{End}_{\mathcal{O}\otimes\mathbb{R}}V_{\mathbb{R}}
. Fix a
good prime

p
as defined in
[
44
, Def. 1.4.1.1]
.

Let
G_{\mathbb{Z}}
be the similitude group over
\mathbb{Z}
defined by assigning any
\mathbb{Z}
-algebra
R
a group
G_{\mathbb{Z}}(R):=\{(g,r)\in\mathrm{GL}_{\mathcal{O}\otimes R}(V_{R})\times\mathbb{G}_{m}(R)|\psi_{R}(gx,gy)=r\psi_{R}(x,y),x,y\in V_{R}\}
. Let
r:=\nu(g)
, the map
\nu
is the similitude character of
G_{\mathbb{Z}}
. We have
G_{\mathbb{Q}}:=G_{\mathbb{Z}}\otimes\mathbb{Q}
is quasi-split and unramified at
p
since
\mathcal{O}\otimes\mathbb{Q}
is unramified at
p
by assumption.

Let
X
be a
G(\mathbb{R})
-orbit of
h_{0}
. Then we form a pair
(G_{\mathbb{Q}},X)
. Note that
G_{\mathbb{Q}}
is not connected in general when
G^{\mathrm{ad}}_{\mathbb{Q}}
has simple factors of type
D
, and
G^{\mathrm{ad}}_{\mathbb{Q}}
might have compact
\mathbb{Q}
-simple factors; however, we can abusively call such a pair
(G_{\mathbb{Q}},X)
a
PEL-type Shimura datum
. Define the
PEL-type Shimura variety with level
K
by the double coset

\mathrm{Sh}_{K}(G_{\mathbb{Q}},X)(\mathbb{C}):=G(\mathbb{Q})\backslash X\times G(\mathbb{A}_{f})/K.

The complex orbifold
\mathrm{Sh}_{K}(G_{\mathbb{Q}},X)(\mathbb{C})
is canonically the analytification of a smooth quasi-projective algebraic variety
\mathrm{Sh}_{K,\mathbb{C}}
over
\mathbb{C}
if
K
is neat.

Assume that
K=K_{p}K^{p}
where
K_{p}=G_{\mathbb{Z}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
is the stabilizer of
V_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}
in
G(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
and
K^{p}\subset G_{\mathbb{Z}}(\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{p})
is a neat open compact subgroup. Those assumptions imply that
K
stabilizes
V_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}}
. Let
\square
be
\emptyset
or
\{p\}
.

Let us denote by
\mathbf{M}_{\mathcal{D},K^{\square}}
the
PEL-type moduli problem
defined as in
[
44
, Def. 1.4.1.4]
by the PEL-type
\mathcal{O}
-lattice
\mathcal{D}
and the level
K^{\square}
; if

K
is neat,
\mathbf{M}_{\mathcal{D},K^{\square}}
is representable by a smooth and quasi-projective scheme over
\spec\mathcal{O}_{F_{0},(\square)}
, where
F_{0}
is the reflex field of
\mathcal{D}
. For any locally Noetherian and connected scheme
S
over
\spec\mathcal{O}_{F_{0},(\square)}
, the objects in
\mathbf{M}_{\mathcal{D},K^{\square}}(S)
consists of the tuples
(\mathcal{A}_{S},\lambda_{S},i_{S},[\varepsilon_{S}]_{K^{\square}})
, where
\mathcal{A}_{S}
is an abelian scheme over
S
with a prime-to-
\square
polarization
\lambda_{S}
and an endomorphism structure

i_{S}:\mathcal{O}\longrightarrow\mathrm{End}_{S}(\mathcal{A}_{S})
, and where
[\varepsilon_{S}]_{K^{\square}}
is an (integral)
K^{\square}
-level structure; the Lie algebra
\underline{\mathrm{Lie}}_{\mathcal{A}_{S}/S}
satisfies the determinantal condition given by
\mathcal{D}
.
Denote by
(\mathcal{A},\lambda,i,[\varepsilon]_{K^{p}})
the universal family over
\mathbf{M}_{\mathcal{D},K^{\square}}
.

On the other hand, the variation of Hodge structures defines a holomorphic family
(\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{hol}},\lambda_{\mathrm{hol}},i_{\mathrm{hol}},[\varepsilon_{\mathrm{hol}}]_{K})
over
\mathrm{Sh}_{K}(\mathbb{C})
. This holomorphic family descends to an algebraic family over
\mathrm{Sh}_{K,\mathbb{C}}
:

Proposition A.1

(
[
43
, p.16]
)
.

There is a canonical open and closed embedding
i:\mathrm{Sh}_{K,\mathbb{C}}\hookrightarrow\mathbf{M}_{\mathcal{D},K,\mathbb{C}}
, such that the holomorphic family
(\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{hol}},\lambda_{\mathrm{hol}},i_{\mathrm{hol}},[\varepsilon_{\mathrm{hol}}]_{K})
over
\mathrm{Sh}_{K}(\mathbb{C})
is canonically isomorphic to the analytification of the pullback of the universal family
(\mathcal{A},\lambda,i,[\varepsilon]_{K})
defined over
\mathbf{M}_{\mathcal{D},K,\mathbb{C}}
by the representability of moduli problem.

A.2.
PEL-type cusp labels

Definition A.2

(
[
44
, Def. 5.4.1.9 and Def. 5.4.2.4]
; see also
[
74
, A.4]
)
.

A
(PEL-type) cusp label
of
\mathbf{M}_{\mathcal{D},K^{\square}}
is denoted by
[(Z_{K^{\square}},\Phi_{K^{\square}},\delta_{K^{\square}})]
, which is an equivalence class of
K^{\square}
-orbits of tuples of the form
(Z,\Phi,\delta)
, where:

•

Z=\{Z_{i}\}_{i={-2}}^{0}
is an admissible and fully symplectic (ascending) filtration on
V_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{\square}}
. We denote by
\gr^{Z}_{i}
the graded pieces defined by
Z
, for
i=0,-1,-2
(see
[
44
, Def. 1.2.6.6 and Def. 5.2.7.1]
);

•

A torus argument
\Phi=(X,Y,\phi,\varphi_{-2},\varphi_{0})
:
X
and
Y
are
\mathbb{Z}
-lattices with
\mathcal{O}
-action,
\phi:Y\hookrightarrow X
is an
\mathcal{O}
-equivariant embedding with finite cokernel,
\varphi_{-2}:\gr^{Z}_{-2}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathrm{Hom}(X\otimes{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{\square}},{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{\square}}(1))
and
\varphi_{0}:\gr^{Z}_{0}\xrightarrow{\sim}Y\otimes{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{\square}}
. The pairing
\langle-,-\rangle_{20}
induced by
Z
and
\psi_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{\square}}
on
\gr^{Z}_{-2}\times\gr^{Z}_{0}
coincides with the pullback of the pairing
\langle-,-\rangle_{\phi}
induced by
\phi
under
\varphi_{-2}\times\varphi_{0}
;

•

\delta
is an
\mathcal{O}
-equivariant splitting
\delta:\bigoplus\limits_{i=-2}^{0}\gr^{Z}_{i}\xrightarrow{\sim}V_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{\square}}
.

Two orbits
(Z_{K^{\square}},\Phi_{K^{\square}},\delta_{K^{\square}})
and
(Z_{K^{\square}}^{\prime},\Phi_{K^{\square}}^{\prime},\delta_{K^{\square}}^{\prime})
are equivalent if
Z_{K^{\square}}=Z^{\prime}_{K^{\square}}
and there is a pair of
\mathcal{O}
-equivariant isomorphisms
(\gamma_{X}:X^{\prime}\xrightarrow{\sim}X,\gamma_{Y}:Y\xrightarrow{\sim}Y^{\prime})
sending
\Phi_{K^{\square}}
to
\Phi^{\prime}_{K^{\square}}
in the sense of
[
44
, Def. 5.4.2.2]
.

The orbit
(Z_{K^{\square}},\Phi_{K^{\square}},\delta_{K^{\square}})
itself is called a
(PEL-type) cusp label representative
of the cusp label
[(Z_{K^{\square}},\Phi_{K^{\square}},\delta_{K^{\square}})]
.

Denote
V:=V_{\mathbb{Z}}\otimes\mathbb{Q}
. By
[
43
, Lem. 3.1.1]
, there is a bijection between admissible
\mathbb{Q}
-parabolic subgroups of
G_{\mathbb{Q}}
and non-trivial minimal admissible symplectic filtrations
W=\{W_{i}\}_{i=-2}^{0}
on
V
; such filtrations are completely determined by their maximal isotropic graded pieces
\gr^{W}_{-2}
.

Let
\mathscr{L}:=(Q,g)
be a pair of an admissible
\mathbb{Q}
-parabolic subgroup
Q
and an element
g\in G(\mathbb{A}_{f})
. Denote by
W
the filtration on
V
determined by
Q
. Following
[
43
, p.21]
, one can associate a PEL-type cusp label with
\mathscr{L}=(Q,g)
:

•

Z^{(g)}_{i}:=g^{-1}(gV_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{\square}}\cap W_{i}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{A}_{f}^{\square})=V_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{\square}}\cap g^{-1}(W_{i}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{A}_{f}^{\square})
. Then
Z^{(g)}_{\bullet}
is actually a filtration defined by
g^{-1}Qg
.
Let
W^{(g)}_{i}:=g^{-1}(gV_{\mathbb{Z}}\cap(W_{i}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{A}_{f}^{\square})).

•

Let
V^{(g)}_{\mathbb{Z}}:=gV_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}}\cap V_{\mathbb{Z}}
. Let
F^{(g)}_{i}:=V^{(g)}_{\mathbb{Z}}\cap W_{i}.

Then
X^{(g)}:=\mathrm{Hom}(\gr^{F^{(g)}}_{-2},\mathbb{Z}(1))
and
Y^{(g)}:=\gr^{F^{(g)}}_{0}
. Note that
V^{(g)}_{\mathbb{Z}}
is equipped with a symplectic pairing:
\psi_{\mathbb{Z}}^{(g)}:V^{(g)}_{\mathbb{Z}}\times V^{(g)}_{\mathbb{Z}}\to\mathbb{Z}(1)
, which is defined by
r^{-1}\psi
, where
r
is the unique
r\in\mathbb{Q}^{\times}_{>0}
such that
r\cdot u=\nu(g)
for some
u\in\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{\times}
.

•

\phi^{(g)}:Y^{(g)}\hookrightarrow X^{(g)}
is induced by the symplectic pairing
\psi^{(g)}_{\mathbb{Z}}
;

\varphi_{-2}^{(g)}
is defined by
\gr^{Z^{(g)}}_{-2}\xrightarrow{u^{-1}\circ\gr(g)}\gr^{F^{(g)}}_{-2}\otimes\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{\square}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathrm{Hom}(X^{(g)}\otimes\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{\square},\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{\square}(1))
and
\varphi_{0}^{(g)}
is defined by
\gr^{Z^{(g)}}_{0}\xrightarrow{\gr(g)}\gr_{0}^{F^{(g)}}\otimes{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{\square}}\xrightarrow{\sim}Y^{(g)}\otimes\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{\square}
.

•

\delta^{(g)}
is any
\mathcal{O}
-equivariant splitting
\delta^{(g)}:\bigoplus\gr^{Z^{(g)}}_{i}\xrightarrow{\sim}V_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{\square}}
.

Denote by
(Z^{(g)}_{K^{\square}},\Phi^{(g)}_{K^{\square}},\delta^{(g)}_{K^{\square}})
the
K^{\square}
-orbit of
(Z^{(g)},\Phi^{(g)},\delta^{(g)})
, and this orbit is a cusp label representative. Then we have constructed a map
\mathrm{CL}^{\square}
, which maps any
\mathscr{L}=(Q,g)
to
\Psi_{\mathscr{L}}:=[(Z^{(g)}_{K^{\square}},\Phi^{(g)}_{K^{\square}},\delta^{(g)}_{K^{\square}})]
.

Now we fix an admissible
\mathbb{Q}
-parabolic subgroup
Q
.
Define a normal
\mathbb{Q}
-subgroup
\widetilde{P}_{Q}
of
Q
as, for any
\mathbb{Q}
-algebra
R
,

\widetilde{P}_{Q}(R):=\{g\in Q(R)|\ g|_{\gr_{0}^{W}}=\mathrm{Id}\text{ and }g|_{\gr_{-2}^{W}}=\nu(g)\mathrm{Id}\}.

When
(G_{\mathbb{Q}},X)
is of Siegel type, we have
\widetilde{P}_{Q}=P_{Q}
by
[
64
, 4.25]
; in general,
P_{Q}\neq\widetilde{P}_{Q}
since there might be compact factors in
\widetilde{P}_{Q}
even in the type A case.

For
\square=\emptyset
or
\{p\}
, define

I_{\mathrm{PEL}}^{\square}(Q):=Q(\mathbb{Q})\widetilde{P}_{Q}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{\square})\backslash G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{\square})/K^{\square}.

Let
G_{l}:=Q/\widetilde{P}_{Q}
. Let
\widetilde{P}_{Q,h}
(resp.
Q_{h}
) be the Levi quotient of
\widetilde{P}_{Q}
(resp.
Q
). Then in the PEL-type case, there is a natural exact sequence

(A.1)

1\to\widetilde{P}_{Q,h}\to Q_{h}\to G_{l}\to 1

and
G_{l}\cong\mathrm{GL}(\gr_{0}^{W})\times\mathrm{GL}(\gr_{-2}^{W})
.

From now on, we
assume
that
G_{\mathbb{Q}}
is connected, that is,
G^{\mathrm{ad}}_{\mathbb{Q}}
(or equivalently,
\mathcal{O}\otimes\mathbb{Q}
) has no simple factors of type
D
.

Lemma A.3
.

Under the assumption above, every PEL-type cusp label representative comes from a
\mathrm{CL}^{p}(Q,g)=\mathrm{CL}(Q,g)
of some admissible
\mathbb{Q}
-parabolic subgroup
Q
and some
g\in G(\mathbb{A}_{f})
.

Proof.

This is
[
74
, Lem. A.4.6 and Prop. A.5.9]
.
∎

Lemma A.4
.

Under the assumption above, the map

G(\mathbb{A}_{f})\to\mathrm{CL}(Q,-)

factors through a bijection
I_{\mathrm{PEL}}(Q)\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathrm{CL}(Q,-)
.

If
\widetilde{P}_{Q}=P_{Q}
, we have
I_{\mathrm{PEL}}(Q)\cong I(Q)
; this is the case when
(G_{\mathbb{Q}},X)
is of Siegel type by
[
64
, 4.25]
. Moreover, in this case, there is a bijection between the set of PEL-type cusp labels and the set of cusp labels defined by
[
64
]
and
[
50
]
.

Proof.

It remains to show the first statement (see
[
24
, B.3]
for a specific case).
Firstly, the map
G(\mathbb{A}_{f})\to\mathrm{CL}(Q,-)
sending
g
to
\mathrm{CL}(Q,g)
factors through
I_{\mathrm{PEL}}(Q)
: for any
p\in\widetilde{P}_{Q}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
,
Z^{(pg)}=Z^{(g)}
by the definition above and
\Phi^{(pg)}=\Phi^{(g)}
by (
A.1
);
q\in Q(\mathbb{Q})
gives a pair of
\mathcal{O}
-equivariant isomorphisms
(\gamma_{X},\gamma_{Y})
. Secondly, assume that, for some
h\in G(\mathbb{A}_{f})
,
\mathrm{CL}(Q,g)=\mathrm{CL}(Q,hg)
. Then up to replacing
h
with
hgkg^{-1}
for some
k\in K
, we have
Z^{(hg)}=Z^{(g)}
so
h\in Q(\mathbb{A}_{f})
. Moreover, there is a pair of
\mathcal{O}
-equivariant isomorphisms
(\gamma_{X},\gamma_{Y})
sending
\Phi^{(g)}_{K}
to
\Phi^{(hg)}_{K}
. Then
h\in Q(\mathbb{A}_{f})
projects to
G_{l}(\mathbb{Q})
via (
A.1
) by the construction of
X^{(g)}
and
Y^{(g)}
. The remaining part of the statement follows from the fact that
Q_{h}\cong\widetilde{P}_{Q,h}\times G_{l}
in the PEL-type case and Lemma
A.3
above.
∎

Appendix B
Isogenies

Let
\square
be a set of prime numbers, which is still
\{p\}
or
\emptyset
in this appendix.

B.1.
Quasi-isogenies

Let
\mathcal{C}
be the category of abelian schemes over
S
, or the category of
1
-motives over
S
.
Recall that we have defined isogenies for
1
-motives; see Definition
2.12
. Then it is also possible to define
\mathbb{Z}_{(\square)}^{\times}
-isogenies for
1
-motives.

Definition B.1
.

Let
\mathcal{Q}_{1}
and
\mathcal{Q}_{2}
be two objects in
\mathcal{C}
.
Let
f:\mathcal{Q}_{1}\to\mathcal{Q}_{2}
be an isogeny. Since
\ker f
is finite flat over
S
, the rank of
\ker f
is locally constant in both cases. We say
f
is a
prime-to-
\square
isogeny
if the rank of
\ker f
over any connected component of
S
is prime to
\square
.

A
\mathbb{Z}_{(\square)}^{\times}
-isogeny

g
from
\mathcal{Q}_{1}
to
\mathcal{Q}_{2}
is represented by a tuple
(f_{1},f_{2},\mathcal{Q}_{3})
consisting of a
\mathcal{Q}_{3}\in\ob\mathcal{C}
and two prime-to-
\square
isogenies
f_{1}:\mathcal{Q}_{3}\to\mathcal{Q}_{1}
and
f_{2}:\mathcal{Q}_{3}\to\mathcal{Q}_{2}
, such that,
g
is the diagram
g:\mathcal{Q}_{1}\xleftarrow{f_{1}}\mathcal{Q}_{3}\xrightarrow{f_{2}}\mathcal{Q}_{2}
. If
f_{1}=[n]
, we denote
g:=\frac{1}{n}f_{2}
. Any two tuples
(f_{1},f_{2},\mathcal{Q}_{3})
and
(h_{1},h_{2},\mathcal{R}_{3})
are equivalent if there is another tuple
(c_{1},c_{2},\mathcal{T}_{3})
such that
c_{1}
and
c_{2}
are prime-to-
\square
isogenies, and there are prime-to-
\square
isogenies
e_{1}:\mathcal{T}_{3}\to\mathcal{R}_{3}
and
e_{2}:\mathcal{T}_{3}\to\mathcal{Q}_{3}
, which fit into the following commutative diagram:

(B.1)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{Q}_{3}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{Q}_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{T}_{3}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{Q}_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{R}_{3}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces e_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces e_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h_{2}}

By Lemma
2.13
in the case of
1
-motives, for any tuple
(f_{1},f_{2},\mathcal{Q}_{3})
representing
g
, there is a tuple
([N],g_{2},\mathcal{Q}_{1})
, for some
(N,\square)=1
and some prime-to-
\square

g_{2}
, that is equivalent to
(f_{1},f_{2},\mathcal{Q}_{3})
.

The
composition
of two
\mathbb{Z}_{(\square)}^{\times}
-isogenies
f=([N_{1}],f_{1},\mathcal{Q}_{1}):\mathcal{Q}_{1}\to\mathcal{Q}_{2}
and
g=([N_{2}],g_{1},\mathcal{Q}_{2}):\mathcal{Q}_{2}\to\mathcal{Q}_{3}
is represented by
g\circ f=([N_{1}N_{2}],g_{1}\circ f_{1}):\mathcal{Q}_{1}\to\mathcal{Q}_{3}
.

By Lemma
2.13
, for any
\mathbb{Z}_{(\square)}^{\times}
-isogeny between
1
-motives
f:\mathcal{Q}_{1}\to\mathcal{Q}_{2}
, there is a prime-to-
\square
isogeny
g:\mathcal{Q}_{2}\to\mathcal{Q}_{1}
and a prime-to-
\square
positive integer
M
such that
f\circ g=[M]
and
g\circ f=[M]
. Hence, we define the
\mathbb{Z}_{(\square)}^{\times}
-isogeny
f^{-1}:=\frac{1}{M}g
to be the
inverse
of
f
. For the case of abelian schemes, see, e.g.,
[
44
, Sec. 1.3.1]
.

In the categorical language, let
T
be the set of arrows consisting of arrows consisting of prime-to-
\square
isogenies in
\mathrm{Mor}(\mathcal{C})
; alternatively, we can let
T
be the set of arrows consisting of all
[N]
, for

N>0
and
(N,\square)=1
. Then the
isogeny category
is defined to be the localization
\mathcal{C}^{\square}:=T^{-1}\mathcal{C}
.

B.2.
Polarizations

B.2.1.

Let
S
be a
{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}
-scheme. Let
\mathcal{Q}
be a
1
-motive over
S
. Denote by
[\mathcal{Q}]
the fppf sheaf representing
\mathcal{Q}
up to
\mathbb{Z}_{(\square)}^{\times}
-isogenies.

For any
f\in\underline{\mathrm{Aut}}_{\mathbb{Z}_{(\square)}}([\mathcal{Q}])
,
f
naturally induces an automorphism
f^{\vee}\in\underline{\mathrm{Aut}}_{\mathbb{Z}_{(\square)}}([\mathcal{Q}^{\vee}])
by the definition of Cartier dual and dual abelian schemes.

Then there is a canonical isomorphism:

(B.2)

\begin{split}\underline{\mathrm{Aut}}_{\mathbb{Z}_{(\square)}}([\mathcal{Q}])&\xrightarrow{\sim}\underline{\mathrm{Aut}}_{\mathbb{Z}_{(\square)}}([\mathcal{Q}^{\vee}]);\\
f&\mapsto f^{\bigstar}:=(f^{\vee})^{-1}.\end{split}

We say a polarization
\bm{\lambda}:\mathcal{Q}\to\mathcal{Q}^{\vee}
is
prime-to-
\square
if it is a prime-to-
\square
isogeny. We say that a
\mathbb{Z}_{(\square)}^{\times}
-isogeny
\bm{\lambda}:\mathcal{Q}\to\mathcal{Q}^{\vee}
is a
\mathbb{Z}_{(\square)}^{\times}
-polarization
if
N\bm{\lambda}
is a prime-to-
\square
polarization for some positive prime-to-
\square
integer
N
.

B.2.2.

Let
\mathcal{B}
be another
1
-motive over a
\mathbb{Z}_{(\square)}
-scheme
S
.
Suppose that there is a prime-to-
\square
isogeny
f:\mathcal{Q}\to\mathcal{B}
. For any
\mathbb{Z}_{(\square)}
-algebra
R
and
z\in\mathrm{End}_{\mathbb{Z}_{(\square)}}([\mathcal{Q}])(R)
,
f
induces an endomorphism
z^{\prime}:=f\circ z\circ f^{-1}\in\mathrm{End}_{\mathbb{Z}_{(\square)}}([\mathcal{B}])(R)
. We will use the notation in §
3.3
.

Lemma B.2
.

There is a unique homomorphism
f_{N}^{\tilde{\gamma},\prime}:\mathcal{B}^{\mathcal{O}_{F}}\longrightarrow(\mathcal{B}^{\mathcal{O}_{F}})^{\oplus|\mathrm{Gal}(F/\mathbb{Q})|-1}
such that the diagram

(B.3)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{Q}^{\mathcal{O}_{F}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{B}^{\mathcal{O}_{F}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathcal{Q}^{\mathcal{O}_{F}})^{\oplus|\mathrm{Gal}(F/\mathbb{Q})|-1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathcal{B}^{\mathcal{O}_{F}})^{\oplus|\mathrm{Gal}(F/\mathbb{Q})|-1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{\tilde{\gamma}}_{N}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{\tilde{\gamma},\prime}_{N}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{\oplus|\mathrm{Gal}(F/\mathbb{Q})|-1}}

commutes. The diagram above induces a prime-to-
p
isogeny
f^{\tilde{\gamma}}:\mathcal{Q}^{\tilde{\gamma}}\longrightarrow\mathcal{B}^{\tilde{\gamma}}
. The isogeny
f^{\tilde{\gamma}}
does not depend on
N
.

Proposition B.3

(cf.
[
33
, Lem. 4.4.8]
)
.

With the setting in Lemma
B.2
, there is a commutative diagram of isomorphisms between fppf sheaves

(B.4)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{Q}^{\tilde{\gamma}}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{F,(p)}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{B}^{\tilde{\gamma}}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{F,(p)}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{Q}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{F,(p)}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{B}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{F,(p)}}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{\tilde{\gamma}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}

Proof.

This follows directly from Lemma
B.2
.
∎
